## Epilogue / 尾声

===Original===

For the first few moments, spent alone by her in her bedroom, Ivy could only feel relief﻿—sheer, sobbing relief.

Then there came over her a sensation of utter, numb despair.

She had lost everything that makes life worth the living to such as she﻿ ﻿…

But there was no time left her, now, to remember the past, or dread the future. She must hurry﻿—hurry.

So it was that, in less than five minutes after Rushworth had left her, she was standing outside the flat, clad in a small pull-on black hat and a big fur coat.

The lift came up, and then, just as she was going to step into it, she remembered suddenly the bolster bag Rushworth had bought for her at Dieppe. On the morning of Jervis’s death she had shaken out of the inner pochette the two or three pinches of﻿—of “stuff” which remained in it, into the fire, and then, hardly knowing what she was doing, she had put it back in the red despatch-box. It would be all right, there, till she went out of her widow’s mourning.

===Chinese===

起初那几分钟，独自待在卧室里的艾薇，心里只觉得解脱——纯粹的、令人啜泣的解脱。

随后，一种彻底的、麻木的绝望感将她整个笼罩。

她已经失去了一切——凡是使人生对她这样的人还值得过下去的东西……

可如今，她已经没有工夫去回想过去，或是惧怕将来。她必须赶紧——赶紧。

就这样，拉什沃思离开她还不到五分钟，她已站在公寓门外，头上是一顶黑色的小套头帽，身上裹着一件宽大的毛皮大衣。

电梯升上来了。可就在她正要跨进去的当口，她突然想起拉什沃思在迪耶普给她买的那只长枕包。杰维斯死去的那天早晨，她曾把内袋里剩下的那两三撮——那点「东西」——抖进火里，然后，几乎不知道自己在做什么，又把它放回了那只红色公文箱。放在那儿是万无一失的，直到她脱下寡妇的丧服。

===Original===

She couldn’t leave *that* behind. Why it was worth a lot of money! Besides, she would give up wearing black as soon as she reached the place of safety Rushworth had promised her.

“Wait a minute,” she said to the porter. “I’ve forgotten something. I won’t be a second!”

She put her key in the lock, and rushed back to her bedroom.

Meanwhile there began an insistent ringing for the lift from the bottom of the shaft, in the hall of the Mansion.

The porter knew pretty Mrs. Lexton’s ways. He felt sure that when she had said: “I won’t be a second!” she meant probably five minutes, maybe even longer than that, especially if she had forgotten something.

The bell was ringing continuously now, and with a shock the man remembered that the agent for Duke of Kent Mansion was coming to see a leak in the roof this very afternoon.

===Chinese===

她可不能把*那个*落下。嗐，它还值好大一笔钱呢！再说，只要一到拉什沃思答应她的那个安全之地，她马上就可以不穿黑衣了。

「等一下，」她对门房说，「我忘了一样东西。一转眼就回！」

她把钥匙插进锁孔，奔回卧室去了。

这时，大厦楼下门厅里，电梯井底响起了一阵急切不停的叫铃。

门房深知漂亮的莱克斯顿太太的做派。他确信，她口中说「一转眼就回！」的时候，多半是指五分钟，说不定还要更久，尤其是她忘了带什么东西的时候。

此刻铃声连绵不绝，他蓦地心头一凛，想起肯特公爵大厦的房产代理今天下午正要来看屋顶的漏处。

===Original===

Quickly he pulled the cable, and the lift slid down.

Meanwhile Ivy had run back into her bedroom, turning up the electric light as she walked through the door. Quickly she took the three keys she always carried about with her in her embroidered black *vanité* case, and, unlocking the half of the great cupboard, she seized the despatch-box.

The lid fell back the wrong side, queerly. Someone failing to force the lock had prized open the hinges, and the bag with its beautiful emerald and pearl clasp was gone﻿—gone!

She threw a wild look round her. What could she take with her? Then she remembered what Rushworth had said. No, she mustn’t take anything. Nothing, apart from that little bolster bag, was of any real value.﻿ ﻿… She turned out the light, and, running blindly through the dark hall, opened the front door of the flat. She hadn’t been more than two or three minutes, after all.

She was trembling now; she felt strung-up and terrified, hardly conscious of what she was doing.

She opened wide the lift gates. They were already ajar, and then﻿—her little feet stepped through into the void.

===Chinese===

他赶紧拉动缆索，电梯滑了下去。

与此同时，艾薇已奔回卧室，进门便拧亮了电灯。她连忙从随身那只绣花的黑色小梳妆包（*vanité*）里取出常带的三把钥匙，打开大挂衣柜上锁的那半边，一把抓起公文箱。

箱盖朝错的一边翻落下去，样子怪诞。有人没能撬开那把锁，便把铰链撬开了，那只缀着漂亮祖母绿与珍珠搭扣的手袋不见了——不见了！

她发狂似的环顾四周。还有什么能带走呢？随即她想起了拉什沃思的话。不，她什么也不许带。除了那只小长枕包，再没有一样东西真正值钱……她关掉电灯，跌跌撞撞地跑过黑暗的过厅，打开公寓的正门。说到底，她前后也不过离开了两三分钟。

这时她浑身发抖，只觉得神经绷紧、心惊胆战，几乎不知道自己在做什么。

她使劲拉开电梯的栅门。那两扇门原本就半开着，接着——她那双小小的脚，一脚踏进了虚空。

===Original===

The man below heard a terrible scream, followed by an awful thud, thud, on the iron top of the lift.

And, at once, with a fearful sensation of dismay, he knew what he had done. He had omitted to shut the gates for the first time since he had been on this job.

For one thing, apart altogether from that little matter of the agent’s visit, he, too, was excited﻿—he, too, had been wondering what was going to happen. Everyone in Duke of Kent Mansion had been thrilled by the news of the reprieve. And, in his excitement at seeing the heroine of the Lexton Mystery, and in his certainty that he would be up again in less than a minute, he had left the lift gates ajar.

In one rushing moment, while on his way to the house-telephone to ring up the engineer, he visualised with dreadful clearness the Coroner’s court, the censure passed on him by the jury, his dismissal from this good situation, and the consequent angry despair of his wife.

Poor, pretty, pleasant-spoken “Ivy,” as he, in the company of thousands of other men of all ages, conditions, and kinds, had fallen into the way of secretly calling her﻿—she had brought bad luck on everybody who came in touch with her. Well! Now she wouldn’t be able to harm anybody, man or woman, any more.

===Chinese===

楼下的人听见一声可怕的尖叫，紧接着，咚、咚，两声可怕的闷响，落在了电梯的铁皮顶上。

他顿觉大祸临头般的惊惶，立刻明白自己干了什么。自打干上这份差事以来，这是他头一回忘了关上栅门。

一则嘛，撇开房产代理要来那桩小事不谈，他自己也正激动着——他也在猜想接下来会发生什么。暂缓行刑的消息，使肯特公爵大厦里人人振奋不已。而正是在见到「莱克斯顿疑案」女主角的激动之中，在笃信自己不到一分钟便会再升上来的把握之中，他让电梯的栅门虚掩着。

在奔往内线电话、要唤技师上来的那一瞬里，种种景象清晰得可怕地在他眼前一闪而过：验尸官法庭，陪审团加给他的申斥，他被这份好差事辞退，以及妻子随之而来的狂怒与绝望。

可怜的、漂亮的、说话客气的「艾薇」——他同成千上万年纪各异、境况各异、形形色色的男人一样，早已惯于在心里这样悄悄唤她——她给每一个同她有过接触的人都带来了厄运。好了！如今她再也害不了任何人了——不论男人，还是女人。

## I / 一

===Original===
The July sun shone slantwise into the ugly, almost sordid-looking bedroom where Ivy Lexton, still only half dressed, had just begun making up her lovely face in front of a tarnished, dust-powdered toilet-glass.

It was nine o’clock in the morning; an hour ago she had had her cup of tea and﻿—mindful of her figure﻿—the hard biscuit which was the only thing she allowed herself by way of breakfast. Her husband, hopelessly idle, easy-natured, well-bred Jervis Lexton, was still fast asleep in the little back bedroom his wife called his dressing-room, but which was their box-room and general “glory-hole.”

Everything that had been of any real value there had gradually disappeared in the last few weeks, for Ivy and Jervis Lexton, to use their own rueful expression, were indeed stony-broke.

Yet they had started their married life, six years before, with a capital of sixty-eight thousand pounds. Now they were almost penniless. Indeed, what Ivy called to herself with greater truth than was usual “her little all,” that is, a pound note, and twelve shillings and sixpence in silver, lay on the stained, discoloured mahogany dressing-table before which she was now standing.

===Chinese===
七月的太阳斜斜地照进那间难看的、简直有些寒碜相的卧室；艾薇·莱克斯顿（Ivy Lexton）衣裳只穿戴了一半，正开始在那面黯淡无光、蒙着粉尘的梳妆镜前，妆点她那张可爱的脸。

时间是早晨九点；一个钟头之前，她喝过了那杯茶，还有——出于对身段的顾念——一块硬饼干，那是她允许自己当作早餐的唯一东西。她的丈夫，那个无可救药地懒惰、性情随和、教养良好的杰维斯·莱克斯顿（Jervis Lexton），还在后面那间小卧室里酣睡。妻子管那间屋子叫他的更衣室，其实那既是他们的储物间，又是个什么都往里堆的「杂物窟」。

近几个星期以来，屋里凡是真正值点钱的东西都已渐渐不见了；因为用他们自己那句酸楚的自嘲来说，艾薇和杰维斯·莱克斯顿当真是彻底破产了。

然而六年前，他们开始婚后生活时，手里握着六万八千镑的本钱。如今他们几乎不名一文。的确，艾薇在心里称作「她的全部家当」的东西——这个说法比她平日的言辞更贴近实情——此刻正摊在她面前那张污渍斑斑、颜色发暗的桃花心木梳妆台上：一张一镑纸币，外加十二先令六便士的银币。她此刻就站在这张梳妆台前。

===Original===
How amazed would her still large circle of friends and acquaintances have been had they learnt how desperate and how hopeless was her own and her husband’s financial position. Yesterday she had even tried to sell two charming frocks brought back for her by a good-natured friend from Paris. But she had only been offered a few shillings for the two, so she had brought them home again.

And now, as her eyes fell on the pound note and tiny heap of silver, they filled with angry tears. How she loathed these sordid, hateful lodgings! What a terrible, even a terrifying thing, it was to have fallen so low as to have to live here, in two shabby, ill-kept bedrooms, where there wasn’t even a hanging cupboard for her pretty clothes, and where the drawers of the painted deal chest of drawers would neither shut nor open.

The Lextons had come there for two reasons. One, a stupid reason, because their landlady was the widow of a man who had been employed as a lad in the stables of Jervis Lexton’s father. A better reason was that, owing to there being no bathroom in the house, the rooms were amazingly, fantastically cheap. The Lextons had already been camping here, as Ivy’s husband put it, for some months, but they rarely gave any of their friends their address. Jervis still belonged to a famous club to which some of his rich men acquaintances would have given much to belong; and Ivy had a guinea subscription to a small bridge club from which her letters were forwarded each day.

===Chinese===
她那些依然为数不少的朋友熟人，若是得知她和她丈夫的财务处境已何等绝望、何等无路可走，该会多么吃惊。就在昨天，她还试着卖掉两条漂亮的女裙，那是一位好心的朋友从巴黎给她捎回来的。可人家两条只肯出几个先令，她只好又把它们带了回家。

此刻，她的目光落在那张一镑纸币和那一小堆银币上，眼里涌起了愤怒的泪水。她多么憎恶这套肮脏可恨的出租房！沦落到这步田地，只能住在这里——两间破旧失修的卧室，连一个挂她那些漂亮衣裳的吊柜都没有，那张漆过的松木五斗柜的抽屉更是关也关不上、拉也拉不开——这是何等可怕、甚至骇人的事啊。

莱克斯顿夫妇搬到这儿来，原因有二。其一是个愚蠢的原因：房东太太是当年杰维斯·莱克斯顿父亲马厩里雇过的一个小伙计的遗孀。更说得过去的理由是，由于屋里没有浴室，房钱便宜得叫人吃惊，简直便宜得离谱。用艾薇丈夫的话说，他们在这里「扎营」已经好几个月了，但他们几乎从不把自己的地址告诉任何朋友。杰维斯仍是一家著名俱乐部的会员，他那些有钱的熟人为了进那家俱乐部，什么代价都肯出；艾薇则在一家小型桥牌俱乐部持有几尼年费的会籍，她的信件每天从那里转递过来。

===Original===
There came a knock at the bedroom door. It was a funny, fumbling knock, and she knew it for that of the landlady’s little boy.

Flinging a pale pink lace-trimmed wrapper round her, “Come in,” she called out sharply.

The child came in, holding in his grubby hand two letters.

She took them from him, and quickly glanced at the envelopes. The one, inscribed in a firm masculine handwriting to her present, Pimlico, address, she put down on the dressing-table unopened. She knew, or thought she knew, so well what it contained.

===Chinese===
卧室门上响起了敲击声。那是一种滑稽的、摸索似的敲法，她听得出这是房东太太的小儿子。

她把一件镶着花边的浅粉色晨衣往身上一裹，尖声喊道：「进来。」

孩子走了进来，脏兮兮的小手里攥着两封信。

她把信接了过来，飞快地扫了一眼信封。其中一封，以一手遒劲的男性笔迹写着她如今在皮姆利科（Pimlico）的地址；她没有拆，径直放在了梳妆台上。她知道——或者自以为知道——那里面装的是什么，知道得太清楚了。

===Original===
There had been a time, not so very long ago, when Ivy Lexton’s beautiful eyes would have shone at the sight of that handwriting. A time when she would have torn that envelope open at once, so that her senses could absorb with delight the ardent protestations of love written on the large plain sheet of paper that envelope contained.

But she no longer felt “like that” towards her daily correspondent, Roger Gretorex. Also she was going to see him this morning in the hope, nay, the certainty, that he would help to tide over this horrid moment of difficulty, by giving her whatever money he could put his hands on.

Gretorex was of a very different stamp from the men who had up to now fallen in love with her. He worshipped her with all his heart and soul, while yet conscious that he was now doing what, before he had been tempted, he would have unhesitatingly condemned in another man. As to that, and other matters of less moment, he was what Ivy Lexton felt to be ludicrously old-fashioned, and she had soon become weary of him, and satiated with the jealous devotion he lavished on her.

Also, Roger Gretorex was poor; not poor as Ivy’s husband had become, largely through his own fault and hers, but through that of his father, a great Sussex squire, who had gambled and muddled away his only son’s inheritance. That was why Gretorex was a doctor, and not what the woman he loved would have liked him to be, an idle young man of means.

The other envelope was addressed in a woman’s flowing hand, and it had been sent on from Ivy’s bridge club.

The writer of the many sheets this thick, cream-laid envelope contained was named Rose Arundell. She was a well-to-do, generous, rather foolish young widow, who had taken a great fancy to lovely Mrs. Jervis Lexton. Mrs. Arundell had been, nay, was, a most useful friend, and a look of dismay shadowed Ivy Lexton’s face as she read on and on, till she reached the end of the long letter.

===Chinese===
就在不算太久以前，还有过那样的时光：一见到那手字，艾薇·莱克斯顿美丽的双眼便会放光。那时她会立刻撕开那只信封，好让自己的身心都饱尝喜悦，去领略信封里那几张朴素的大白纸上写满的炽热爱情的表白。

可是如今，她对这位每天给她写信的罗杰·格雷托雷克斯（Roger Gretorex），已经不再有「那样的」感觉了。况且今天上午她就要去见他，怀着希望——不，是怀着十拿九稳的把握——指望他能把自己凑得出来的钱都交给她，帮她渡过这个可憎的难关。

格雷托雷克斯和至今拜倒在她裙下的那些男人属于截然不同的类型。他全心全意地崇拜她，心里却又明白，自己如今做的事，若在未受诱惑之前，换作别的男人，他会毫不迟疑地加以谴责。在这一点上，以及其他不那么要紧的事情上，他在艾薇·莱克斯顿看来都迂腐得可笑；她很快就腻烦了他，对他倾泻在她身上的那种带着醋意的深情，也感到餍足生厌。

再者，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯很穷；不过他的穷不像艾薇丈夫的那样——那多半是他自己和她两个人的过错——他的穷是他父亲的罪过。他的父亲是萨塞克斯（Sussex）的一位大乡绅，赌博厮混，把独子的家业败了个精光。正因如此，格雷托雷克斯才当了医生，而没有成为他心爱的女人所希望的那种人——一个有产业、闲散度日的年轻人。

另一只信封上是女人洒脱流丽的笔迹，是从艾薇的桥牌俱乐部转寄来的。

这只厚实的布纹信封里装着好几页信笺，写信人名叫罗丝·阿伦德尔（Rose Arundell）。她是一位家境宽裕、出手大方、头脑却相当糊涂的年轻寡妇，对可爱的杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太喜欢得了不得。阿伦德尔太太过去是——不，现在仍然是——一位极其有用的朋友；可是艾薇·莱克斯顿一页页读下去，直读到这封长信的末尾，脸上却罩上了一层愕然的阴云。

===Original===
> Ivy, darling, I have the most *astounding* news to tell you!

> I’ll begin at the beginning. Besides, I can’t help thinking﻿—for I know you’re rather worried just now, poor dear﻿—that it may be of help to *you*. D’you remember my telling you last time we met at that tiresome fête where we couldn’t see each other for a moment alone, that I’d had a *wonderful* adventure? That I’d been to a fortune-teller? Her name is Mrs. Thrawn. She lives at No. 1 Ranelagh Reach on the Embankment. Her fee is a pound﻿—and I feel inclined to send her a thousand pounds when I think of what she has done for me!

> I don’t mind telling you *now* that I was on the point of taking that silly boy, Ronny. No one knows but myself how *horribly* lonely I’ve been. Well, I thought I’d go and see this Mrs. Thrawn and hear what she’d got to say; for, after all, I didn’t *love* Ronny, and I always had a dreadful suspicion it was my *money* he liked, rather than *me*.

> Well, my dear, I went off *trembling*. But I can’t tell you how wonderful she was! She described Ronny and *warned me* against him. Then she said that an extraordinary change was coming over my life, and that if I would only be patient and *wait*, everything I had most longed for would come to pass. She was most awfully kind﻿—*really* kind. She said that if I was sensible and did what she said﻿—I mean *refuse* Ronny﻿—I should take a long journey very soon to a place that she, Mrs. Thrawn, knew well and loved; and that I should be very, *very* happy there. That place was India, as I knew, for the woman who first told me about Mrs. Thrawn said she was the widow of a *missionary*!

===Chinese===
> 艾薇，亲爱的，我有一件*石破天惊*的消息要告诉你！

> 我就从头说起吧。再说，我不禁想——我知道你这阵子正烦心呢，可怜的宝贝——这说不定对*你*也有帮助。还记得我们上次在那场烦死人的游园会上碰面吗？那时连单独说一句话的工夫都没有。我跟你说，我遇上了一件*奇妙的*历险，说我去找过一个算命的？她名叫索恩太太（Mrs. Thrawn），住在河滨大道（the Embankment）拉尼勒里奇1号（No. 1 Ranelagh Reach）。她的卦金是一镑——一想到她为我做成的事，我简直想给她寄一千镑去！

> 到了*现在*，我也不妨对你说了：我那时正差一点要接纳那个傻小子罗尼（Ronny）。除了我自己，谁也不知道我一向寂寞得*多么可怕*。唉，我就想，去见见这位索恩太太，听听她怎么说；因为说到底，我并不*爱*罗尼，而且我心里总有一个可怕的疑影：他喜欢的是我的*钱*，而不是*我*这个人。

> 于是，亲爱的，我便*浑身发抖*地去了。可她有多神妙，我简直没法对你说！她把罗尼的模样形容了出来，还*警告我*提防他。接着她说，我的生活里将有一场非同寻常的巨变，只要我沉得住气、肯*等待*，我最渴盼的一切都会成真。她人和气极了——是*真正*地和气。她说，只要我识相，照她的话去做——我是说*回绝*罗尼——我不久就会作一次长途旅行，去一个她索恩太太又熟悉又热爱的地方；到了那里，我会非常、*非常*幸福。那地方是印度，这我心里有数，因为最早跟我说起索恩太太的那位太太说过，她是一位*传教士的*遗孀！

===Original===
> And then, oh, Ivy, what do you think happened? I wonder if you remember all I told you about the soldier who was my first love? The man whom my mother would not let me marry and who did so splendidly in the war? He’s home on leave from India, where he has a *splendid* appointment. We ran across one another in the street, and I asked him to come and see me. You can guess the rest!

> His leave is up *by the end of next week*. We shall be married very quietly on Thursday, and sail for *India on Friday*.

> I’m in a whirl, as you can imagine. I’d love to have *you* at my wedding, darling, for you really *are* my dearest friend. But *he* doesn’t want anyone there who didn’t know us both in the *old* days, before the war. He hasn’t a bean, but, thank God, I’ve *plenty* for us both!

> Your *devoted*

> Rose.

===Chinese===
> 再后来，哎呀，艾薇，你猜出了什么事？不知你还记不记得，我从前跟你讲过的那个军人，我的初恋？就是我母亲不肯让我嫁、后来在战争中大放异彩的那个人？他正从印度回国休假，在那边他有一份*了不起的*差事。我们在街上不期而遇，我就请他来看我。剩下的你猜也猜得到了！

> 他的假期*到下周末*便满了。我们将于星期四悄然成婚，并于*星期五启程赴印度*。

> 你可想而知，我这会儿正晕头转向呢。亲爱的，我巴不得*你*能来参加我的婚礼，因为你确实*是*我最亲爱的朋友。可是*他*不愿意让任何战前*老*日子里不认识我们俩的人在场。他身无分文，不过感谢上帝，我的钱*多的是*，够我们两个人用的！

> 你的*忠实的*

> 罗丝。

===Original===
Ivy Lexton put the long letter she had just read down on the dressing-table. Then she took up the other, still unopened, envelope, and stuffed it into her bag. After all she could read the letter it contained in the omnibus, on her way to see Roger Gretorex. He had taken over for a friend a slum practice in Westminster, and he lived in what Ivy called a horrid little street named Ferry Place.

She turned again towards the looking-glass, and began once more making up her face at the point where she had been interrupted. She was so used to the process that she worked quickly, mechanically, though taking a great deal of intelligent care, far more care than did most of her young married women friends.

With regard to everything that concerned herself, Ivy Lexton was quick, uncannily shrewd, and instinctively clever. She knew how to exploit to the very best advantage her exceptional physical beauty, her natural charm of manner, and, above all, her extraordinary allure for men.

And yet, so unsuspicious is human nature in that stratum of the financially, easy, agreeably self-absorbed, and pleasure-loving world in which Ivy played a not unimportant part, that all the men, and many of the women, who came across her in that world, would have told you that Ivy Lexton was “a dear little thing,” “a regular sport,” “a good plucked one,” and “a splendid wife to that rotter Lexton.”

===Chinese===
艾薇·莱克斯顿把刚读完的长信放在梳妆台上。然后她拿起另一只尚未拆启的信封，塞进了手提包。反正去见罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的路上，她可以在公共汽车里读那封信。他替一位朋友代管着威斯敏斯特（Westminster）一处贫民区的诊所，自己住在一条被艾薇唤作讨厌的小街里，街名叫费里街（Ferry Place）。

她重新转向镜子，从被打断的地方接着妆扮起来。这道工序她做惯了，手上又快又机械，却又处处用心，讲究的程度远在她那些年轻已婚的女朋友们之上。

凡是牵涉到自己的事，艾薇·莱克斯顿反应敏捷、精明得近乎邪门，还有一股天生的机灵劲儿。她懂得怎样把自己出众的美貌、天生的仪态风韵，尤其是她对男人那种异乎寻常的吸引力，利用到十足十的地步。

然而，在那个手头宽裕、安逸自得、只顾自己、耽于享乐的社会圈子里——艾薇在其中也扮演着一个并非不重要的角色——人心竟是不设防的；在那个圈子里遇到过她的男人，还有许多女人，都会告诉你：艾薇·莱克斯顿是「个讨人喜欢的小可人儿」「一个地道的爽快人」「一个有胆量的好姑娘」，还是「那个无赖莱克斯顿的了不起的好太太」。

===Original===
When she had finished what was always to her an interesting and pleasant task, she stood still, and did nothing, for a moment. She longed to get away from this hateful room and this horrible house, yet Roger Gretorex would not be free of his poorer patients for quite a long while. This was the more tiresome as she always went into his tiny mid-Victorian house by the back way, through the surgery, which gave into a blind alley.

Suddenly her eyes fell on “her little all.” Why shouldn’t she take that pound note, and call on Rose Arundell’s wonderful fortune-teller on her way to Ferry Place? After all, she, too, might have an unexpected bit of luck waiting for her round the corner.

She slipped on a cool pale-pink cotton frock given her by that same generous friend who was now, to her regret, going out of her life. Then she jammed a little brown straw hat on her fair, naturally wavy, shingled head, and, tiptoeing down the carpetless stairs, she hurried through the dirty hall into the sunshiny street.

===Chinese===
她做完了这件在她永远是饶有兴味、令人愉快的差事，便静静地站了一会儿，什么也没做。她巴不得马上离开这间可憎的屋子、这所讨厌的房子，可是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯还得好一阵子才打发得完他那些穷病人。这愈发叫人心烦，因为她每次都是打后门进他那栋维多利亚时代中期的小房子，要穿过那间通进死胡同的诊室。

忽然，她的目光落在了「她的全部家当」上。何不带上那张一镑纸币，在去费里街的半道上，去拜访一下罗丝·阿伦德尔那位神奇的算命太太呢？说不定，转眼之间也有份意想不到的好运气正等着她呢。

她套上一条清凉的浅粉色棉布裙——正是那位慷慨的朋友送的，而此人如今眼看就要从她的生活里退场了，她不免觉得可惜。接着她把一顶棕色小草帽往她那头金色的、天然鬈曲的短发上一扣，踮着脚走下没有地毯的楼梯，匆匆穿过肮脏的门厅，走进了阳光明媚的街道。

---

===Original===
Ranelagh Reach consisted of a row of six early nineteenth-century houses on that part of the Embankment which forms a link between Westminster and Chelsea. Two of the houses had evidently been taken over lately by well-to-do people, for they had been repainted, and their window-boxes were now filled with ivy-leaved geraniums. The four other houses were shabby-looking and dilapidated, and it was in one of these that there dwelt the woman who had taken as her professional name that of Janet Thrawn. The blinds of No. 1 were down, the brown paint on the front door had peeled, and the steps had evidently not been “done” for days. Everything looked so poverty-stricken that Ivy felt surprised when a very neat and capable-looking maid opened the door in answer to her pull at the old-fashioned bell. She had expected to see a slatternly little girl.

“I’ve come to see Mrs. Thrawn; Mrs. Arundell sent me.”

“I’m not sure that Mrs. Thrawn can see you, miss, unless you’ve made an appointment. But please come in, while I go and see.”

The inside of the little house was in its way as much of a surprise as the maid; it was very different from what the outside would have led the visitor to expect. There was a fine Persian rug on the floor of the narrow hall, and plenty of light came in from a window halfway up the staircase. Affixed to the red walls were plaster casts of hands, forming a curious, uncanny kind of decoration.

===Chinese===
拉尼勒里奇是河滨大道上一排六幢十九世纪初期的房子，地处连接威斯敏斯特与切尔西（Chelsea）的那一段。其中两幢显然新近被有钱人接了手，重新油漆过，窗箱里如今栽满了藤叶天竺葵。另外四幢则破旧寒酸、年久失修，那位以珍妮特·索恩（Janet Thrawn）为职业化名的女人，就住在其中一幢里。1号的百叶窗垂着，前门上的棕漆已经剥落，台阶分明多日没有「打扫」过了。样样都显得穷相毕露，因此当一位十分整洁、看上去挺干练的女仆应着她拉响的老式门铃前来开门时，艾薇不免觉得意外。她原以为会见到一个邋遢的小丫头呢。

「我来见索恩太太；是阿伦德尔太太叫我来的。」

「小姐，要是您没有预先约好，我不敢说索恩太太能不能见您。不过请进来吧，我去问问看。」

这小屋的内部，和那女仆一样，自有叫人意外之处；它与外表留给访客的预想大相径庭。狭窄门厅的地板上铺着一块上好的波斯地毯，楼梯半腰的一扇窗子透进充足的光线。红色的墙上固定着一只只手的石膏模型，构成一种古怪而瘆人的装饰。

===Original===
After the maid had gone upstairs Ivy Lexton felt a sudden impulse “to cut and run.” A pound note meant a great deal to her just now. But as she was turning towards the front door, the woman came down the steep stairs of the old house.

“Mrs. Thrawn will see you,” she said. Then she turned and preceded the visitor up the staircase.

As they reached the landing the maid murmured:

“Mrs. Thrawn won’t be a moment.”

Ivy Lexton looked round her nervously. There were evidently two rooms on this floor﻿—the front room, of which the blinds were down, and a back room, of which the door was masked by a heavy embroidered green silk curtain. On the patch of wall which formed the third side of the landing was a dark oil painting, bearing on its tarnished gold frame the inscription in black letters, “The Witch.” The subject was that of a white-haired woman being burnt alive, while an evil-looking crowd gloated over the hideous sight.

===Chinese===
女仆上楼之后，艾薇·莱克斯顿突然生出一股「撒腿就跑」的冲动。一张一镑的纸币，此刻在她可不是小数目。可她正要转身朝前门走去，那女仆已经顺着老房子陡峭的楼梯下来了。

「索恩太太愿意见你。」她说。说完便转过身，领在访客前头上楼去了。

两人走到楼梯口时，女仆低声道：

「索恩太太马上就来。」

艾薇·莱克斯顿紧张地四下张望。这一层显然有两间屋子——前面一间百叶窗垂着，后面一间的房门被一幅厚重的绿绸绣花帷帘遮着。楼梯口第三面墙上挂着一幅色调幽暗的油画，黯淡的金框上以黑字题着「女巫」二字。画的是一个白发女人正被活活烧死，一群面目邪恶的人对着那可怖的景象幸灾乐祸。

===Original===
There came the tinkle of a bell.

“Mrs. Thrawn will see you now,” said the woman shortly, drawing back the curtain to show a door already ajar.

“Come in!” called a full, resonant voice.

Feeling excited and curious, for this was the first time she had ever been to a fortune-teller, Ivy brushed past the maid.

Then she felt a pang of disappointment. The room before her was so very ordinary﻿—just an old-fashioned back drawing-room, containing one or two good pieces of furniture, while on the chimneypiece stood a row of silver-gilt Indian ornaments.

===Chinese===
传来一阵铃铛的叮铃声。

「索恩太太现在就可以见你了。」女仆简短地说，一面拉开帷帘，露出一扇已经半开的门。

「进来！」一个浑厚响亮的声音唤道。

艾薇又兴奋又好奇——这是她生平头一回到算命的人家里来——从女仆身边擦身而过。

随即她感到一阵失望。眼前这间屋子实在太平常了——不过是一间老式的后客厅，摆着一两件像样的家具，壁炉台上立着一排镀金银的印度小摆设。

===Original===
Even the soothsayer, the obvious owner of this room, impressed her client as being almost commonplace. At any rate there was nothing mysterious or romantic about her appearance. She was a tall, powerful-looking woman, nearer sixty than fifty. Her grey hair was cut short, and she was clad in an old-fashioned tea-gown, of bright blue cashmere, which fell from her neck to her feet in heavy folds.

The most remarkable feature of Mrs. Thrawn’s face was her eyes. They were light hazel, luminous, compelling eyes, and as Ivy Lexton advanced rather timidly towards her they became dilated, as if with a sudden shock of gripping, overwhelming surprise.

Yet nothing could have appeared at once more simple and more attractive than this lovely girl who wanted to take a peep into the future. Ivy Lexton looked almost a child in her flesh-coloured cotton frock and the simple pull-on brown hat which framed her exquisite little face.

Making a determined effort over herself, Mrs. Thrawn withdrew her astonished and, indeed, affrighted, glance from her visitor, and said coldly, “I cannot give you long this morning, for I have an appointment”﻿—she looked at her wristwatch﻿—“in twenty minutes. I suppose you know my fee is a pound, paid in advance?”

===Chinese===
就连这位卜卦人——显然是这间屋子的主人——给她的主顾的印象，也几乎是平平无奇。至少她的外表毫无神秘或浪漫可言。她是个高个子、看上去很有气力的女人，年纪说五十倒不如说更近六十。灰白的头发剪得短短的，身上穿着一件老式茶会长袍，是亮蓝色的开司米料子，从颈项一直垂到脚边，褶层厚重。

索恩太太的脸上最不同寻常的，是那双眼睛。那是双浅榛色的眼睛，晶莹透亮，有一股不由分说的慑力；当艾薇·莱克斯顿怯生生地朝她走近时，那双眼睛忽然睁大了，仿佛被一阵攫住心神的、排山倒海般的惊愕击中了似的。

然而，再没有比这位想窥探一下未来的可爱姑娘更显得又单纯又动人的了。艾薇·莱克斯顿穿着那条肉色棉布裙，戴着一顶随手套上的简朴棕色软帽，帽子衬着她那张精致的小脸，看上去简直像个孩子。

索恩太太狠了狠心，才把那惊愕的、甚至可以说是惊骇的目光从访客身上收回来，冷冷地说道：「今天上午我不能多给你时间，因为我有个约会」——她看了看腕表——「二十分钟后就到。我想你知道我的卦金是一镑、须得预付的吧？」

===Original===
Ivy felt a touch of resentment. Only twenty minutes for a whole pound? Yet she was beginning to feel the compelling power of the woman, and so, slowly, she took the one-pound note that remained to her out of her bag.

Mrs. Thrawn slipped the note into one of the patch pockets of her gown, and motioned her visitor to a low stool, while she sat down, herself, in a big armchair opposite. For a moment Ivy felt as she had felt when as a little girl she was going to be scolded.

“We will begin with your hands. No! Not like that. Your left hand first, please, and the back to start with.”

As she took Ivy’s hand in her cool firm grasp Mrs. Thrawn said quietly, “I need not tell you that you have amazing powers of﻿—well, keeping your own counsel, when it suits you to do so.”

Then she turned the hand she held over, and taking a small lens out of the pocket where now lay Ivy’s one-pound note, she closely scrutinised the lines crisscrossing the rosy palm.

===Chinese===
艾薇觉得有点气恼。整整一镑钱，只换二十分钟？可她已经开始感觉到这个女人身上那股不由分说的威力，于是慢吞吞地把剩下的那张一镑纸币从手提包里取了出来。

索恩太太把纸币塞进长袍的一只贴袋里，示意客人坐到一张矮凳上，自己则在对面一把大扶手椅里坐了下来。有一瞬间，艾薇恍惚觉得自己回到了小时候快要挨骂的光景。

「我们先从你的手看起。不！不是那样。请先伸出左手，先看手背。」

索恩太太用她凉爽而坚定的手握住艾薇的手，平静地说：「用不着我告诉你，你有一种惊人的本事——唔，就是把心事守在自己肚子里，只要你觉得那样合适。」

接着她把手翻了过来，从那只如今躺着艾薇一镑纸币的口袋里取出一枚小小的放大镜，细细端详那只红润掌心里纵横交错的纹路。

===Original===
“You’ve the most extraordinary fate-line that I’ve ever seen﻿—and that’s saying a very great deal,” she observed.

“What I want to know,” began Ivy eagerly, “is﻿—”

“Whether there is going to be any change for the better in your life?”

The fortune-teller waited a moment, and, lifting her head, she gave her client a long measuring look. “Yes, there is going to be a great change in your life. But as to whether it will be for the better or for the worse﻿—?”

Mrs. Thrawn hesitated for what seemed to the other a long time. But at last she exclaimed, “From your point of view I should say ‘for the better,’ for I see money, a great deal of money, coming your way.”

===Chinese===
「你这条命运线，是我平生所见最不寻常的——这话的分量可不轻。」她说。

「我想知道的是，」艾薇急切地开口道，「是——」

「是不是你的生活将会有什么好转？」

算命太太略等了片刻，然后抬起头，朝主顾投去久久的、掂量似的目光。「是的，你的生活将有一场大变。至于这变是好是坏——？」

索恩太太迟疑了很久，久得让对方觉得有些难熬。但末了她高声道：「依你的眼光看，我该说是『变好』，因为我看见钱——好大一笔钱——正朝你这边来了。」

===Original===
Ivy turned crimson, so great were her surprise and joy.

“Will it be soon?” she asked eagerly.

“Very soon﻿—in a few hours from now.”

“Are you sure?”

“Quite sure.”

===Chinese===
艾薇的脸涨得通红，她是又惊又喜。

「快了吗？」她急切地问。

「很快——就在几个钟头之内。」

「你确定吗？」

「十分确定。」

===Original===
Mrs. Thrawn lifted her great head, and again she looked at her visitor fixedly.

“May I speak plainly? Will you try not to be offended at what I’m going to say?”

“Nothing you say could offend me,” cried Ivy in her prettiest manner. “You don’t know how happy you’ve made me!”

“I do know. But, though I don’t suppose you will ever believe it, money is not everything, Mrs.﻿—”

===Chinese===
索恩太太昂起她那颗大脑袋，又目不转睛地端详起这位访客来。

「我可以有话直说吗？待会儿我要说的话，请你尽量别见怪。」

「你说什么也不会得罪我的。」艾薇拿出她最娇媚动人的态度嚷道，「你可不知道你让我有多高兴！」

「我当然知道。不过呢——尽管我看你这辈子也不会信——钱并不是一切，太太——」

===Original===
“﻿—Lexton.”

The name slipped out. After all, why shouldn’t she tell Mrs. Thrawn her name? Yet she was sorry she had done so a few moments later, for the fortune-teller, leaning forward, exclaimed harshly:

“Now for the powder after the jam! I sense that you are engaged in an illicit love affair fraught to you, and to others also, with frightful danger.”

Once more Ivy’s face crimsoned under her clever makeup, but this time with fear and dismay. Her eyes fell before the other woman’s hard scrutiny.

===Chinese===
「——莱克斯顿。」

姓氏就这么脱口而出。说到底，为什么不能告诉索恩太太自己的名字呢？可几分钟之后她就后悔了，因为那算命太太身子往前一探，厉声说道：

「蜜饯吃过了，接着该上药粉了！我察觉到你正陷在一桩不正当的私情里，这桩私情给你、也给旁人，带来的是可怕的危险。」

艾薇那张精心妆扮的脸又一次涨红了，可这一回是因为恐惧和惶乱。在那女人冷硬的逼视之下，她的眼睛垂了下去。

===Original===
“Wrong is, of course, a matter of conscience, and I know you think you have nothing to be ashamed of. But you are leading a fine soul astray, and evil influences are gathering round you.”

“I know that I’ve done wrong,” faltered Ivy, frightened and perplexed by Mrs. Thrawn’s manner, rather than by her warning.

The other said sharply, “You know nothing of the sort! You’ve not got what I call a conscience, Mrs. Lexton. But a conscience nowadays is a very old-fashioned attribute. Many a young woman would hardly know what to do with one if she had it!”

Ivy did not know what to answer, and felt sorry indeed that she had let this censorious, disagreeable person know her name.

===Chinese===
「说到罪过，那当然是良心上的事，我也知道你自问没有什么见不得人的地方。可是你正在把一个美好的灵魂引上邪路，种种邪恶的势力正在你四周聚拢。」

「我知道我做错了。」艾薇嗫嚅道。让她心惊意乱的，倒不是那番警告，而是索恩太太的神态。

对方尖刻地说：「你根本不知道什么错不错的！你身上没有我所说的那种良心，莱克斯顿太太。不过呢，良心如今也是件老古董了。多少年轻女人，就算给了她一颗良心，她也未必知道拿它派什么用场！」

艾薇不知如何作答，心里实在懊悔，不该让这么个好苛责、讨人嫌的家伙知道了自己的姓名。

===Original===
“For your own sake,” went on Mrs. Thrawn earnestly, “break with this man who loves you. For one thing, ‘it’s well to be off with the old love before you are on with the new.’ ”

“Then there is going to be another man in my life?” Ivy asked eagerly.

“I see a stranger coming into your life within a few hours from now. Whether his valuable friendship for you endures will entirely depend on yourself.”

Mrs. Thrawn got up from her chair.

===Chinese===
「为你自己着想，」索恩太太恳切地接着说，「跟这个爱你的男人断了吧。一来，『旧情未断，莫结新欢。』」

「那么说，我的生活里还要再出现另一个男人？」艾薇急切地问。

「我看见一个陌生人，将在几个钟头之内走进你的生活。他那份于你弥足珍贵的情谊能否长久，全然取决于你自己。」

索恩太太从椅子上站了起来。

===Original===
“As we haven’t much time, I will now look into the crystal.”

She drew down the blind of the one window in the room, and, going across to the writing-table, she took off it a heavy, round glass ball which looked like, and might indeed have been sold for, a paperweight. Then, moving forward a small, low table, she put it between herself and her visitor.

“Don’t speak,” she said quickly. “Try to empty your mind of all thought.”

Bending her head, she gazed into the crystal, and what seemed to Ivy Lexton a long time went by.

In reality, it might have been as long as two minutes before Mrs. Thrawn began speaking again, this time in a quick, muffled voice.

===Chinese===
「时间不多了，我现在就来看水晶球。」

她放下屋里那扇独窗的百叶帘，走到写字台前，从上面捧起一只沉甸甸的圆玻璃球——那样子活像个镇纸，说不定当初就真是当镇纸卖出去的。然后她把一张小矮桌往前挪了挪，摆在客人和自己中间。

「别出声。」她急急地说，「尽量把心里的一切念头都排空。」

她低下头，凝望进水晶球里；在艾薇·莱克斯顿看来，过去了好长一段时间。

实际上，大约过了有两分钟之久，索恩太太才重新开口，这一回声音急促而低沉。

===Original===
“I see you both now, you and the dark young man on whom you will bring unutterable misery and shame, and who will bring you distress and disappointment, if you do not break with him now, today. The safe way is still open to you, Mrs. Lexton, but soon it will be closed, and you will find yourself in a prison of your own making, and trapped﻿—trapped like a rat in a sinking ship.”

Again there was a long, tense silence, and again Ivy began to feel vaguely frightened.

The prediction of shame and misery to another meant very little, if indeed anything, to her. But distress and disappointment to herself? Ah! that was another thing altogether. Ivy very much disliked meeting with even trifling disappointments.

Mrs. Thrawn looked up. All the brilliance had gone out of her curious, luminous eyes.

===Chinese===
「现在我看见你们两个了，看见你，也看见那个黝黑的年轻人。你若不现在、就在今天跟他断绝，你将给他带来难以言说的痛苦和耻辱，而他也将带给你烦恼和失望。稳妥之路眼下还向你敞开着，莱克斯顿太太，可不久它就要关上了，你会发现自己置身于一座自己亲手筑成的牢狱里，被死死困住——像沉船上的一只老鼠那样被困住。」

又是一阵长久而紧绷的沉默，艾薇又开始莫名地感到害怕。

说别人将蒙受耻辱和痛苦，这话在她心里实在没多少分量，甚至压根没有分量。可是她自己将遭遇烦恼和失望？啊！那可完全是另一回事了。艾薇这个人，连芝麻大的失意都极不喜欢碰上。

索恩太太抬起头来。她那双奇异的、晶莹发亮的眼睛里，光彩已消失殆尽。

===Original===
“I fear you will not follow the better way,” she said slowly. “Indeed, I sense that you are making up your mind not to follow it, unless the doing so falls in with your other plans. I see this dark young man’s destiny closely intertwined with your life. He will bear the scars you are about to inflict on him to his grave, and that whether he lives but a few months, or a long lifetime. You do not what you call love him any more. But he loves you as you have never yet been loved, and never will be.”

Her voice softened and became low and pitiful, for the girl who was now gazing at her with a surprised, frightened expression on her exquisite face looked too young to be what the soothsayer believed her to be, that is, already doomed, unless she altered her whole way of life, to suffer terrible things.

“As woman to woman, let me give you a word of advice, Mrs. Lexton. For your own sake try to follow it.”

“I will!” cried Ivy sincerely.

===Chinese===
「恐怕你是不会走那条更好的路了。」她缓缓地说，「的确，我觉察到你正在打定主意不走，除非走那条路恰好合乎你别的盘算。我看见这个黝黑的年轻人的命运，正和你的生活紧紧纠缠在一起。你即将加在他身上的那些伤疤，他会带进坟墓里去——不管他只能再活几个月，还是活上漫长的一辈子。你已经不再——用你们的话说——爱他了。可是他爱你的那种爱，是你从来没有领受过的，也永远不会领受到的。」

她的声音柔和下来，变得低沉而悲悯；因为此刻正睁着一双又惊又怕的眼睛望着她的这个姑娘，那张精致的小脸显得太年轻了，实在不像卜卦人心目中的那种人——也就是说，不像一个已经注定了厄运的人：除非她把整个生活方式改弦更张，否则可怕的劫难就要临头。

「女人对女人，我送你一句忠告吧，莱克斯顿太太。为了你自己，请尽力照着做。」

「我一定照办！」艾薇诚心诚意地喊道。

===Original===
“Do not be afraid of poverty﻿—” And then, as she saw the other’s instinctive recoil, “Poverty does not touch the likes of you with its cold finger,” and Mrs. Thrawn gave an eerie laugh. “If you are wise, if you do what is still open to you to do, you will have ups and downs, but the ups will predominate, and there will always be some man, even when you become what I should call an old woman, who will be proud, yes, proud, to be your banker.”

“Do you see something nice coming for me soon in that glass ball?” asked Ivy nervously.

She longed, secretly, to be told something more of the new man who was coming into her life.

Mrs. Thrawn bent over the cloudy crystal. Then she muttered:

===Chinese===
「不要怕穷——」话没说完，她看见对方本能地一缩。「贫穷那根冰冷的手指，可碰不着你这样的人。」索恩太太发出一声怪异的笑。「你要是聪明，趁那条路还敞着就照着去做，你这一生会有起有落，但起的时候多；而且总会有那么一个男人——哪怕到了你成为我所说的老太婆的时候——会引以为荣，是的，引以为荣，来当你的银行家。」

「你在那玻璃球里，看见有什么好事快要临到我头上了吗？」艾薇忐忑地问。

她暗暗巴望着，能再多听几句关于那个将要走进她生活的新的男人的事。

索恩太太俯身对着那团云翳般的水晶。然后她喃喃地说：

===Original===
“The pictures are forming. They are coming thick and fast. And﻿—but no, I will not tell you what I see, for what I am seeing may not concern you at all. It may concern the future of the woman who is now on my doorstep﻿—”

And, as she said the word “doorstep,” the old-fashioned house-bell pealed through the house.

Mrs. Thrawn rose and put her crystal back on the writing-table. Then she pulled up the blind.

“We’ve only a few moments left. But I’m going, for my own satisfaction,” she interjected in a singular tone, “to tell your fortune by the cards.”

===Chinese===
「影像正在成形。它们来得又密又急。还有——不，我看见的东西不能告诉你，因为我此刻看见的，也许跟你毫不相干。它也许关系到此刻正站在我门前台阶上的那个女人的将来——」

正说到「门前台阶」这个词，那老式的门铃便叮铃铃地响彻了整所房子。

索恩太太站起身，把水晶球放回写字台上。然后拉开了百叶帘。

「我们只剩几分钟了。不过，为了让我自己称心，」她用一种异样的口吻插话说，「我再用纸牌给你算一卦。」

===Original===
As she spoke she took a pack of cards out of the drawer of her writing-table, and sank down again into her chair.

“Now, cut.”

After Ivy had obeyed, the soothsayer rapidly dealt out the cards. Then she put down her finger on the queen of hearts.

“This card stands for you,” she dragged her finger along. “And here is the king of diamonds, the man who is coming into your life, and who will give you money, much money. Even so﻿—” she shook her head, “you will never be able to count on him as you can count on the man who is still bound to you, and whom I bid you cast out of your life at once﻿—at once.”

===Chinese===
她一面说，一面从写字台的抽屉里取出一副纸牌，重新坐回椅子里。

「来，切牌。」

艾薇依言切了牌，卜卦人飞快地把牌摊开。然后她把手指按在了红心王后上。

「这张牌代表你。」她的手指顺势划过去。「这边是方块国王，那个将要进入你的生活的男人，他会给你钱，很多钱。尽管如此——」她摇了摇头，「你永远不能像信赖那个至今仍和你拴在一起的男人那样信赖他；而那个男人，我吩咐你立刻把他从你的生活里赶出去——立刻。」

===Original===
She swept the cards together and rose from her chair.

“I saw trouble in your hand; I saw trouble in the crystal; I saw great trouble in the cards. Yet, Mrs. Lexton, you are not a woman who troubles trouble before trouble troubles you. Even so, unless you follow my advice about your present lover, I see misfortune galloping towards you like a riderless horse.”

“But you do still believe that I’m going to get a lot of money?” Ivy asked pleadingly. “Did the cards tell you that also?”

“Yes, the cards told me that also.”

===Chinese===
她把牌拢作一堆，从椅子里站起身来。

「我在你手上看见了祸事；我在水晶球里看见了祸事；我在纸牌里看见了大祸。不过，莱克斯顿太太，你可不是那种祸事还没上门、就先自为祸事发愁的女人。话虽如此，除非你听从我对你眼下这位情人的忠告，我看见厄运正像一匹脱了缰的马，朝你飞奔而来。」

「可是你确实还是相信，我会得到一大笔钱，对吗？」艾薇恳求似的问，「纸牌也这样对你说了吗？」

「是的，纸牌也这样告诉我了。」

===Original===
From outside the door came the sound of footsteps.

“One last word﻿—one last warning. When you came into this room you were not alone, Mrs. Lexton.”

Ivy stared at her. What could Mrs. Thrawn mean? Of course she had been alone!

“You were accompanied, surrounded, by a huge mob of men and women, invisible to you, but visible to me. Are you an actress?”

===Chinese===
门外传来了脚步声。

「最后一句——最后一次警告。你走进这间屋子的时候，并不是独自一人，莱克斯顿太太。」

艾薇直愣愣地瞪着她。索恩太太这话是什么意思？她当然是一个人来的！

「有一大群男女陪着你、围着你，你看不见他们，我却看得见。你是个演员吗？」

===Original===
“I was an actress, for a little while, before I married,” said Ivy, smiling. “And I’d love to go back on the stage, but only as a leading lady, of course.”

“Given certain eventualities, you will become of great moment, of absorbing interest, to hundreds of thousands of people. Men and women will fight over you﻿—the newspapers will record your every movement.”

Ivy smiled self-consciously. This last unexpected prediction gave her a thrill of pleasurable excitement. What could it mean but a triumphant return to the stage, of which she had been hitherto only a humble and transient ornament?

“There is a woman already in your life﻿—I see her now standing behind you. She is a grey-haired, worn-looking old woman. If you fail to do what I advise you to do, she will play an overwhelming part in your destiny. Indeed it is she who may determine your fate.”

===Chinese===
「结婚以前，我当过一阵子演员。」艾薇微笑着说，「我也乐意重返舞台，不过当然，只做女主角。」

「倘若日后种种机缘凑合，你将变成千千万万人心头举足轻重、人人争睹的人物。男男女女将为你争执不下——报纸将记录你的一举一动。」

艾薇有些忸怩地笑了。这最后一个出人意料的预言，让她体验到一阵又愉快又兴奋的震颤。这不就是说，她将凯旋般重返舞台吗？迄今为止，她不过是舞台上一个无足轻重、转瞬即逝的小点缀而已。

「你的生活里已经有了一个女人——我此刻就看见她站在你身后。她是个灰白头发、操劳憔悴的老妇人。你若不照我的忠告去做，她将在你的命运里扮演一个压倒一切的角色。说实在的，决定你命运的，很可能正是她。」

===Original===
Then she turned, and taking a tiny silver-gilt bell off the mantelpiece she rang it sharply.

The door opened, and the maid pulled aside the heavy curtain. There was no stranger waiting on the landing. Ivy looked so surprised that the woman smiled. “Mrs. Thrawn doesn’t like her clients to cross one another. The lady who has just come in is waiting in the front room.”

As Mrs. Thrawn’s late visitor walked quickly down the Embankment towards the place for which she was bound, she felt more really lighthearted than she had felt for, oh! such a long time. Money coming her way﻿—and a new man in her life? That was all Ivy Lexton really remembered of that curious interview. The warnings Mrs. Thrawn had given her she put down to the soothsayer’s conventional outlook on life.

===Chinese===
然后她转过身，从壁炉台上拿起一只小小的镀金银铃，急促地摇响了。

门开了，女仆拉开那幅厚重的帷帘。楼梯口并没有什么生人等候。艾薇一脸的讶异，那女仆不由得笑了。「索恩太太不喜欢她的主顾们彼此打照面。刚才来的那位太太，在前面的屋子里候着呢。」

索恩太太这位方才问过卦的访客，快步沿着河滨大道朝她要去的那个地方走去，心里觉得说不出的轻松，比多少日子以来——哎呀！——都要轻松。钱要来了，还有一个新的男人要进入她的生活？这便是艾薇·莱克斯顿对那场古怪会面真正记得的全部了。至于索恩太太给她的那些警告，她一股脑儿归之于算命人对世事的那套老生常谈。

===Original===
As for the woman’s advice concerning Roger Gretorex, she ought to have known, being a fortune-teller, that she, Ivy, had already made up her mind to break with her secret lover. She could not, however, break with him today, for two reasons. First, she was going to ask him for a little money, and secondly, he was giving a theatre-party this evening. She, Ivy, her friend Rose Arundell, and Jervis Lexton were to be Gretorex’s guests, and he was taking them on, after the play, to supper at the Savoy. That had been settled days ago.

Rose Arundell? She told herself vexedly that Rose would certainly “chuck.” In fact it was plain that Rose had forgotten all about tonight’s engagement, or she would have mentioned it in her letter. They would be three instead of four. But perhaps, after all, that didn’t really matter, for Jervis was quite fond of Roger.

There was, however, a fly, albeit a small fly, in the ointment. There was no such person, there never had been, or, it seemed to her, could be, in her life, as a worn-looking, grey-haired woman. This fact made her feel a little doubtful, a little anxious, as to the truth of the fortune-teller’s other predictions.

===Chinese===
至于那女人关于罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的忠告，她身为算命的，本该早已知道，她艾薇早已打定主意，要和这位秘密情人一刀两断了。只是今天还不能断，原因有二。其一，她还要向他讨一点钱；其二，他今晚要作东请人看戏。她艾薇、她的朋友罗丝·阿伦德尔，还有杰维斯·莱克斯顿，都是格雷托雷克斯请的客人，散了戏他还要带他们去萨伏伊饭店（the Savoy）吃夜宵。这是几天前就定好了的。

罗丝·阿伦德尔吗？她气恼地暗自断定，罗丝准要打退堂鼓的。事实上明摆着，罗丝把今晚的约会忘了个一干二净，否则她在信里不会不提。他们将要三个人，而不是四个人了。不过也许，这终究算不得什么，反正杰维斯挺喜欢罗杰。

不过，这炉香膏里终究掉进了一只苍蝇——虽说是只小苍蝇。她的生活里没有这样一个人物，从来就没有过，照她想来也绝不可能有：一个灰白头发、操劳憔悴的老妇人。这个事实，让她对那算命太太其余预言的真实性，隐隐生出了几分疑虑、几分不安。

## II / 二

===Original===
“Why, there’s Mr. Rushworth! Do go over and ask him to join us for coffee.”

Ivy Lexton was smiling at her husband﻿—a delicious, roguish smile. As he smiled back, he told himself with conscious satisfaction that his little wife was far the prettiest woman here tonight.

Her sleekly brushed-back auburn hair, white skin, violet eyes, and slender rounded figure were wont to remind those few of her admirers familiar with the art of Romney of a certain portrait of Nelson’s Emma, spinning.

Ivy’s husband was pleasantly aware that she was not only the prettiest, but also one of the smartest looking, of the women supping at the Savoy tonight. This was the more commendable, from simple Jervis Lexton’s point of view, as they were so hard up﻿—stony-broke, in fact.

===Chinese===
“咦，那不是拉什沃思先生吗？快过去请他过来，跟我们一起喝杯咖啡。”

艾薇·莱克斯顿（Ivy Lexton）正朝丈夫微笑——笑得俏皮，动人极了。丈夫回她一笑，心里颇带几分得意地想道：今晚在场的女客当中，自家这位小妻子实在是远远出众的美人。

她一头赤褐色的头发梳得溜光，向后紧紧拢起；肌肤雪白，眼睛是紫罗兰色，身段纤秾合度——她那少数几位熟稔罗姆尼（Romney）画艺的仰慕者，每每由她想起那幅画着纳尔逊的艾玛纺纱的肖像。

今晚在萨伏伊饭店（Savoy）用宵夜的女客里，艾薇的丈夫满心欢喜地看得明白：她不但是最美的，也是仪容装扮最入时的几位之一。在老实的杰维斯·莱克斯顿（Jervis Lexton）看来，这就更加难能可贵，因为他们如今手头拮据得很——说白了，已经是彻底的穷光蛋。

===Original===
He generally did at once anything Ivy asked him to do, but now he waited for a few moments.

“D’you mean that chap we met at the Hamptons? *I* don’t see him.”

“Don’t be stupid, darling! He’s over in that corner, with two dowdy-looking women, looking bored to death. I’m sure he’ll be delighted to join us for coffee.”

There had come an edge of irritation in her seductive voice. Ivy had a peculiar and very individual intonation, and many a man had found it the most enchanting voice in the world.

===Chinese===
平日里艾薇叫他做什么，他向来立时照办，这回却耽搁了片刻。

“你是说咱们在汉普顿家碰见的那家伙？*我*可没瞧见他。”

“别犯傻了，亲爱的！他就在那边角落里，陪着两个穿得寒碜的女人，正闷得要死呢。请他过来一块儿喝咖啡，他准高兴。”

她那勾人的嗓音里已透出一丝焦躁。艾薇的语调自成一格、极有个性，多少男人曾把那当作世上最销魂的声音。

===Original===
At last Lexton rose, just a thought unwillingly. He had been enjoying himself tonight, forgetting the money anxieties which had at last become desperately pressing, while listening to his wife’s gay chatter concerning the well-known people who were also supping at the Savoy this July night.

The young man liked a party of just three friends much better than the big noisy suppers to which he sometimes escorted his wife. In a way it was funny that their host, a grim-looking young doctor named Roger Gretorex, was their friend, for even Lexton realised that, though he and Gretorex were both country-bred, and belonged to the now vanishing old county gentry class, they had nothing else in common.

The host had hardly said a word during the whole evening, either at the theatre, or since they had come on here. So taciturn had he been that Lexton supposed the poor chap was glum because the pretty widow, Mrs. Arundell, whom they were to have brought with them to make a fourth, had fallen out. He knew that Ivy suspected that Gretorex liked Mrs. Arundell quite a little bit. If so, the young man was out of luck, for he was the last sort of chap an attractive widow was likely to fancy.

===Chinese===
莱克斯顿终于起了身，心里多少有点不情愿。今晚他过得正惬意——一边听着妻子兴致勃勃地闲聊，聊的是同在这七月夜里来萨伏伊用宵夜的各路名人，一边把那些终究已逼到眉睫、火烧火燎的钱财烦忧暂且忘了个干净。

比起有时要陪妻子去赴的那些闹哄哄的大型宵夜宴，这年轻人更喜欢区区三两知己的小聚。说来也有趣，今晚做东的竟会是他们的朋友——一位神情冷峻的年轻医生，名叫罗杰·格雷托雷克斯（Roger Gretorex）；因为连莱克斯顿自己也明白，他和格雷托雷克斯虽同为乡间长大，同属那个日渐消亡的旧日郡绅阶层，除此之外却再无半点共通之处。

一整晚，这位东道几乎没开过口，不论在戏院里，还是到了这里以后。他沉默寡言到了这般田地，莱克斯顿便猜想，这可怜的家伙之所以郁郁不乐，多半是因为原本约好同来凑成四人的那位漂亮寡妇阿伦德尔太太（Mrs. Arundell）临时失了约。他知道，艾薇疑心格雷托雷克斯对阿伦德尔太太颇有些意思。若真如此，这年轻人可就没了指望，因为他正是风韵撩人的寡妇最不可能瞧上眼的那种男人。

===Original===
As he threaded his way between the crowded round tables, there came over Jervis Lexton a queer and very definite feeling of unwillingness to obey his wife. He remembered that he and Ivy had met this man, Miles Rushworth, about three weeks ago at a weekend country house party, and that Ivy had taken quite a fancy to him. But he, Jervis, remembered that he himself had not taken to Rushworth. For one thing, he had thought the man too damn clever and pleased with himself.

Rushworth was, however, an enormously rich man, and Ivy had spent most of the Saturday evening of that long weekend out in the moonlit garden, pacing up and down with him. Late that same night she had told her husband, excitedly, that her new friend had said he thought he could find what Lexton had long been looking for﻿—an easy, well-paid job.

But Ivy’s husband, simple though he might be, had learnt a thing or two since they had joined the ranks of what some call “the new poor.” One was that, though his wife could twist most men round her little finger, it didn’t follow she could make them do much to help him. This time he had been so far wrong that on the Monday morning this chap Rushworth, after motoring them both back to town, when saying goodbye had muttered something to Ivy as to “setting your husband on his feet again.” But this had happened a good three weeks ago, and their new acquaintance had given no further sign of life.

===Chinese===
在一张张拥挤的圆桌之间穿行时，杰维斯·莱克斯顿心头泛起一股古怪而又分明的情绪：不愿听妻子的差遣。他记得，他和艾薇约莫三个星期前在一次乡间宅邸的周末聚会上认识了这位迈尔斯·拉什沃思（Miles Rushworth），艾薇对他相当着迷。可他杰维斯记得，自己却没喜欢上拉什沃思。一则是，他觉得这人聪明得讨嫌，还自鸣得意。

不过拉什沃思是个家赀巨万的阔人。那个长长的周末里，星期六晚上大半的时光，艾薇都是同他在月光下的花园里踱步度过的。当晚夜深时分，她兴奋地告诉丈夫，这位新朋友说，他寻摸得到莱克斯顿长久以来一直想找的那样东西——一份轻松体面、薪水优厚的差事。

然而艾薇的丈夫尽管头脑简单，自打沦入有些人所谓的“新穷人”的行列，也长了一两个心眼儿。其中一条便是：他妻子纵然能把大多数男人玩弄于股掌之上，却未必就能让他们为他出多大的力。这一回他就想岔到了头——那个星期一的早晨，这位拉什沃思开车送他们俩回城，临分手时曾含含糊糊地对艾薇嘀咕过几句，说是要“让你丈夫重新站稳脚跟”。可那已是足足三个星期以前的事了，这位新交此后便再无半点音信。

===Original===
---

After Lexton had risen from the little round table, the two he had left sitting there kept silence for what seemed, to the woman, a long time. Then suddenly Gretorex exclaimed, in a low tense voice, “How I hate hearing you call that man ‘darling’!”

Ivy Lexton made no answer to that statement. She was picking up the tiny crumbs left by her fairy bread from the side of her plate, and arranging them in a diamond pattern on the tablecloth. But, though she seemed intent on her babyish task, she was angrily, impatiently aware that her companion was gazing at her with unhappy, frowning eyes. His words had cut across her pleasant thoughts﻿—her joyful relief at having seen Miles Rushworth, at knowing that in a few moments he would be here, with her.

“I’m sorry I made this plan about tonight,” she said at last, scarcely moving her lips. “It was stupid of me.”

===Chinese===
---

莱克斯顿从那张小圆桌旁起身之后，被他撇下的两个人一直沉默着；在那女子看来，这沉默持续得可真久。忽然，格雷托雷克斯低沉而紧绷地迸出一句：“听你管那个男人叫‘亲爱的’，我心里有多恨！”

对这句话，艾薇·莱克斯顿未作任何回答。她正把仙女面包（fairy bread）吃剩在盘边的细屑一粒粒拈起来，在桌布上摆出一副菱形花样。她看似专心做着这项孩子气的活计，却又气又急地觉察到，同伴正蹙着眉、满眼怏怏地盯着她。他的话截断了她心里那股愉快的念头——她方才瞧见了迈尔斯·拉什沃思，正欢欢喜喜地松了一口气，知道不消几分钟他就会来到这里，来到她身边。

“今晚这个安排，是我出的主意，真对不起，”她终于开口，嘴唇几乎没动，“是我糊涂。”

===Original===
“It was more than stupid of me to agree to it,” muttered Gretorex savagely. “I’ve never been more wretched in my life than I’ve been tonight!”

She told herself, with a touch of contempt, that what he had just said was not only stupid, but utterly untrue. Why should the presence of her good-humoured, easygoing husband make Roger wretched? But she kept her feeling of irritation in check.

She glanced round at him﻿—it was a pleading, tender glance﻿—and his heart leapt. How wondrous beautiful she was, and﻿—how divinely kind!

===Chinese===
“答应这安排的我就更不止是糊涂了，”格雷托雷克斯恶狠狠地低声道，“我这一辈子，还没像今晚这样痛苦过！”

她暗自带着几分轻蔑想道：他方才这话，不光糊涂，而且全然不是实话。她那位好脾气、好说话的丈夫在场，凭什么会让罗杰痛苦？但她把心头的恼意强按了下去。

她回头朝他望了一眼——那是含着恳求的温柔一瞥——他的心猛地一跳。她是何等惊人的美丽，而且——心地是何等天使般的仁厚！

===Original===
And then a curious thing happened to Roger Gretorex. In that softly illumined, flower-scented, luxurious London restaurant, it was as if he saw in a vision the wistful and plain, if intelligent, face of a girl he had known the whole of her short life. Her name was Enid Dent, and she was now twenty-one. Had he not met Ivy Lexton seven months ago, he and Enid Dent would now have been engaged to be married.﻿ ﻿…

So strong was the half-hallucination that he shut his eyes. When he opened them again the vision was gone, and he was hearing the voice which meant more to him than any other voice would ever mean to him in this world murmur gently, “It hasn’t been exactly cheerful for me.”

Impulsively he exclaimed, “You’re an angel, and I’m a selfish brute, Ivy﻿—”

She smiled, but it was a mirthless smile.

===Chinese===
就在这时，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯身上发生了一桩奇事。在这灯影柔亮、花香氤氲、奢华舒适的伦敦餐馆里，他恍恍惚惚，如在幻象中看见了一张少女的脸——虽则相貌平平，却透着聪慧，带着渴慕。这姑娘他从她幼年便认识；她名叫伊妮德·登特（Enid Dent），今年二十一岁。倘若七个月前他没有遇见艾薇·莱克斯顿，此刻他和伊妮德·登特早已订了婚约。……

这半是幻觉的景象如此真切，他不由得闭上了眼睛。待再睁开时，幻象已经消散，他听见那个声音——这世上再没有哪个声音，能像它一样对他意味深长——正温柔地低语：“我今晚也算不上怎么快活。”

他一时冲动，脱口喊道：“你是个天使，我却是个自私的畜生，艾薇——”

她莞尔一笑，却是一丝笑意也无的笑。

===Original===
“Not a brute, Roger, only just a little selfish. I was a fool to ask you to ask us tonight﻿—”

For the first time this evening Ivy Lexton had uttered a few true, sincere words. She knew now that it had been a stupid act on her part to bring her husband and this strong-natured, not over good-tempered, young man who loved her together this evening. But after all they had to meet now and again! Poor Jervis quite liked Roger Gretorex. Why couldn’t Roger like Jervis, too?

Ivy was really fond of her husband. He was so kindly, so unsuspicious, on the whole so easy to manage, and still so absolutely devoted to her. And yet of late she often thought, deep in her heart, what a glorious life she might be leading now, if Jervis, less or more devoted, had granted her, two years ago, an arranged divorce. There had been a rich young man who had adored her. But Jervis had angrily refused to fall in with her scheme. It had led, in fact, to their only real quarrel. But “all that” was now forgiven and forgotten.

===Chinese===
“算不得畜生，罗杰，只是有点儿自私罢了。今晚要我去求你把我们请来，才是我糊涂——”

今晚艾薇·莱克斯顿这是头一回说了几句真心实意的话。她如今才明白，今晚把自己的丈夫和这个性子刚烈、脾气欠佳、爱慕着她的年轻人凑到一处，实在是她干的一桩蠢事。可话又说回来，他们总免不了隔三差五要见见面呀！可怜的杰维斯还挺喜欢罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，罗杰为什么就不能也喜欢杰维斯呢？

艾薇是真心喜欢自己丈夫的。他心地那么厚道，对人毫不猜疑，大体上又那么容易摆布，而且至今对她死心塌地。然而近来，她心底常常暗暗思量：倘若两年前杰维斯——不管忠心几分——肯依她办一场安排妥帖的离婚，她如今过的该是何等称心快活的日子！那时有一个阔绰的年轻人痴恋着她。可杰维斯勃然大怒，拒不容应她的盘算。事实上，那是他们夫妻间唯一一场真正的争吵。不过“那些事”如今都已原谅、都已忘怀了。

===Original===
She stole a look at the occupants of the other tables and, as she did so, she felt a sharp stab of envy. They all seemed so prosperous, so carefree! Each woman had that peculiar, indefinable appearance which only a happy sense of material security bestows, each man, in his measure, looked like a lord of life.﻿ ﻿… But what was this Roger Gretorex was saying as he bent towards her? “I sometimes wonder if you really know, dearest, how much I love you?”

The ardent words were whispered low, but she heard them very clearly, and she smiled. Though she was growing very weary of Roger Gretorex, it is always sweet to a woman to feel she is loved as this man loved her.

Still, she felt relieved when she saw her husband, and the three she had sent him for, threading their way through the narrow lane left between the beflowered tables.

===Chinese===
她偷眼打量邻桌的座上客，一边打量，一边感到一阵尖锐的嫉妒的刺痛。他们个个显得那么优裕，那么无忧无虑！每个女人脸上都有一种说不清道不明的神采，唯有物质上高枕无忧的幸福感才能赋予；每个男人也各按各的分量，一派人生主宰的气象。……可是，俯身凑近她的罗杰·格雷托雷克斯这时在说什么来着？“我有时候不免怀疑，最亲爱的，你究竟明不明白，我有多爱你？”

那一番炽热的话是低声吐出的耳语，她却听得字字分明，于是笑了。尽管她对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯早已腻烦透顶，可是一个女子被这样一个男人爱着，这份感觉终究是甜的。

不过，等到望见丈夫领着她差他去请的那三位，正从摆满鲜花的桌案之间留出的窄道里穿行而来，她还是如释重负。

===Original===
Miles Rushworth was leading the little company. He was the kind of man who always does lead the way. Though he was now only two or three tables off, Ivy realised that he had not yet seen her, and so she was able to cast on him a long measuring glance.

Mary Hampton, the woman at whose house they had met, had said that he was a millionaire. The word millionaire fascinated Ivy Lexton. And then all at once she told herself that it was Rushworth, of course it must be, who was the stranger coming into her life.

Miles Rushworth was tall and well built but, had he not kept himself in good condition, he would have been a stout man. He had a healthy, almost a ruddy, complexion; brown eyes; what is called a good nose; a large, firm mouth; and perfect teeth. His short-clipped brown hair was already slightly streaked with grey, though he was only thirty-six.

He was not in, and did not care to be in, what to herself Ivy called “society.” Neither was he nearly so much a man of the world as was, for instance, her own rather foolish husband. Yet Miles Rushworth had that undeniable air of authority, that power of making himself attended to at once, which always spells brains and character, as well as what old-fashioned folk call a good conceit of oneself.

===Chinese===
迈尔斯·拉什沃思走在这一小队人的最前头——他天生就是那种永远领头的人。虽然此刻离她不过两三张桌子远，艾薇看出他还没瞧见她，于是得以送过去长长的一瞥，把他细细估量了一番。

玛丽·汉普顿（Mary Hampton）——他们初次就是在她家相识的——说过，他是位百万富翁。“百万富翁”这个词，听得艾薇·莱克斯顿心痒难搔。跟着她忽然对自己说：闯进她生活里来的那个陌生人，就是拉什沃思——当然是他，一定不错。

迈尔斯·拉什沃思高挑个子，体格结实；不过若非一直勤于将养，他本会发福成个大胖子。他气色健康，近于红润；一双褐色眼睛；一只所谓端正的好鼻子；一张大而坚毅的嘴；一口无可挑剔的牙齿。他那剪得短短的褐色头发已经微微掺了灰白，虽然他不过三十六岁。

他并不置身于——也无意置身于——艾薇私下称作“社交界”的那个天地。论处世阅历，他也远远赶不上她那相当愚蠢的丈夫。然而迈尔斯·拉什沃思自有那股不容置疑的威严气度，有那种叫人顿时凝神屏息的本事——这向来意味着头脑和魄力，也意味着老派人所说的“自视甚高”。

===Original===
She glanced also, with quick scrutiny, at Rushworth’s guests. They were probably a mother and daughter, and, though dowdily dressed, obviously well-bred women. The older lady was wearing a black lace gown of antiquated make; the lace was caught at her breast with an early-Victorian brooch made of fine diamonds. Hung round her long, thin neck was an emerald necklace. The girl had a pleasant, animated face, and a good figure. Her long hair was still dressed as it had been when she was eighteen﻿—a fact that marked her age as being about seven- or eight-and-twenty. She was wearing an unbecoming pale mauve dress, and there came over Ivy a fear that she might be a widow. Lovely Ivy Lexton shared the elder Mr. Weller’s opinion concerning widows.

The younger lady’s only ornament was a string of real pearls. The pearls, though not large, were beautifully matched.

As Miles Rushworth came close up to the table, Ivy Lexton rose from her chair, and her face broke into an enchanting expression of pleasure and welcoming surprise. As she held out her hand she exclaimed: “Jervis felt sure it was you! Thank you so much for coming over here. It is most kind of your friends to come too.”

Rushworth took her little hand in his strong grasp. He gazed down into her upturned face with a look which, to her at least, proved she had not been mistaken, and that, in spite of his broken promise, already she meant something to him.

===Chinese===
她又目光如梭，飞快地审视了拉什沃思的同伴们。那多半是一对母女，穿着虽然寒素，一望便知是教养良好的体面女子。年长的那位穿一件老式样的黑蕾丝长裙，胸前用一枚维多利亚早期的精美钻石胸针别住蕾丝；细长的脖颈上挂一串翡翠项链。那姑娘有一张讨人喜欢的、生动活泼的脸，身段也端正。她的长发仍照着她十八岁那时的式样梳理——这一条便表明，她的年纪约在二十七八之间。她穿一条不衬她的淡紫色连衣裙；艾薇忽然起了一层疑惧：她莫非是个寡妇？美丽的艾薇·莱克斯顿，对寡妇的高见，与《匹克威克外传》里那位老韦勒先生（Mr. Weller）如出一辙。

年轻女士身上唯一的饰物，是一串货真价实的珍珠。珠子虽不大，却搭配得精美绝伦。

迈尔斯·拉什沃思走近桌边，艾薇·莱克斯顿从椅上起身，脸上顿时绽开一副迷人的神情，又惊又喜，满含欢迎。她一边伸出手，一边喊道：“杰维斯就料定是你！多谢你肯赏光过来。你的朋友们也一同前来，真是太好不过了。”

拉什沃思伸出有力的手，握住她那只小手，俯视着她仰起的脸——那一副眼神，至少向她证明了她没有看错人；尽管他失过约，她于他而言，已经有些分量了。

===Original===
He turned round: “May I introduce my friend Mrs. Lexton, Lady Dale?” And then, more lightly, he exclaimed: “Bella, I want you to know Mrs. Lexton!”

As she held out her hand, “Bella” smiled and looked, with unenvious admiration, at the lovely young woman before her. This pleased Ivy, for she had an almost morbid desire that all those about her should like her, feel attracted to her, and think well of her, whatever their relation to herself might happen to be.

A moment later Bella Dale found herself sitting next to a gloomy-looking young man who somehow interested her because he looked clever, as well as gloomy. Jervis Lexton was talking pleasantly, happily, to Lady Dale. As for Miles Rushworth, he had lowered himself into a chair which he had unceremoniously seized from another table, and which he had put a little apart from the rest of the party, and close to Mrs. Lexton.

===Chinese===
他转过身来：“请允许我介绍，这位是我的朋友莱克斯顿太太——戴尔夫人（Lady Dale）。”接着又轻快地喊道：“贝拉（Bella），我要你认识莱克斯顿太太！”

“贝拉”伸出手来，微微含笑，怀着不掺一丝妒意的欣赏，打量面前这位可爱的年轻女子。这正合艾薇的心意，因为她有一种近乎病态的欲求：凡围绕在她身边的人，不论与她是什么关系，都得喜欢她，倾心于她，说她好。

不多时，贝拉·戴尔发觉自己挨着一位神情阴郁的年轻人坐下了；不知怎的，这人反倒让她起了兴致，因为他看上去既阴郁，又聪明。杰维斯·莱克斯顿正同戴尔夫人谈得愉快而欢畅。至于迈尔斯·拉什沃思，他早已一屁股坐进一把椅子——那椅子是他毫不客气地从别桌硬搬来的，又挪得离众人略略分开，紧挨着莱克斯顿太太。

===Original===
“I have forgotten all you told me, and what I promised you,” he said in a low tone. “But I only came back to town this morning, and I’ve been fearfully busy all the time I’ve been away.”

He waited a moment, then he asked her what she felt to be a momentous question. “Would your husband take a job away from London?”

A feeling of acute dismay swept over her. It would be dreadful if this big powerful man﻿—powerful in every sense﻿—were to arrange suddenly that she and Jervis should go to live in some dreary, dull town in the north of England! So, after a perceptible pause, she answered frankly, “I don’t think I should like to leave London, and as for my husband, I’m afraid he’d be like a fish out of water, anywhere else.”

===Chinese===
“你对我说过的那些话，还有我应承你的事，我全忘了，”他低声说，“不过我是今儿早上才回城的，离开的这些日子，一直忙得不可开交。”

他略停了停，随后问了她一句她觉得干系重大的话：“你丈夫肯不肯接受伦敦城外的工作？”

一阵尖锐的沮丧袭遍她的全身。倘若这个高大强壮的男人——不论从哪一层意义上讲都强壮——突然做了安排，叫她和杰维斯到英格兰北部哪个死气沉沉的乏味小城去住，那才叫糟糕呢！于是，在一段分明可察的停顿之后，她坦率地答道：“我看我不愿意离开伦敦；至于我丈夫，换个地方，恐怕他就成了离水的鱼。”

===Original===
Miles Rushworth looked across to where Jervis Lexton was now sipping slowly a liqueur brandy. “The chap looks a regular slacker,” he said to himself contemptuously.

He considered it a tragic thing that the deliciously pretty, sweet-natured, little woman now sitting so close to him that they nearly touched, should be married to “that.”

He heard her whisper hesitatingly, “But Jervis must get something to do very soon now, Mr. Rushworth, or I don’t know what we shall do. We’re so horribly hard up,” and her mouth, that most revealing feature, quivered.

His strong face﻿—the face he believed to be so shrewd, and which was shrewd where “business” was concerned﻿—became filled with warm sympathy.

===Chinese===
迈尔斯·拉什沃思朝对面望去——杰维斯·莱克斯顿正在那儿慢慢啜着一小杯白兰地甜酒。“这家伙，活脱脱一个好吃懒做的闲人。”他轻蔑地暗自说道。

他觉得这实在是一桩惨事：这么个娇艳动人、性情温柔的小女人，此刻坐得离他这般近，两人几乎身子相贴，竟然嫁给了“那号人”。

他听见她迟疑地低声道：“可是杰维斯得赶快寻个事做才成，拉什沃思先生，不然真不知道我们该怎么办了。我们手头拮据得吓人。”她那张最藏不住心绪的嘴，微微发颤。

他那张坚毅的脸——一张他自信精明过人的脸，论起“生意经”来也确实精明——此刻盈满了温暖的怜惜。

===Original===
“That can’t be allowed to go on!” he exclaimed a little awkwardly.

During their last moonlit walk and talk in the dark, scented garden of the house where they had first met, Ivy Lexton had told him the pathetic story of her life. How, when she and Jervis Lexton had first married, they had been quite well off, but that a dishonest lawyer had somehow muddled away all “poor Jervis’s money.”

She had further confessed that now they were really “up against it,” hard-driven as they had never been before.

===Chinese===
“这种光景，可不能再容它这样下去了！”他喊道，口气略显笨拙。

在他们初次相遇的那座宅子里，两人最后一次在幽暗芬芳的花园里月下并肩漫步、絮絮交谈时，艾薇·莱克斯顿向他讲了自己那令人心酸的身世。她说，她和杰维斯·莱克斯顿新婚之时，家境原本十分宽裕，不料一个奸猾的律师不知怎么一来，竟把“可怜的杰维斯的”那些钱折腾了个精光。

她还进一步吐露：如今他们是真的“走投无路”了，被逼到了从未有过的窘迫地步。

===Original===
“An idle man,” she had said, speaking in that tremulous, husky voice which nearly always touched a listener’s heartstrings, “can’t help spending money. I would give anything to get my husband a job!”

Miles Rushworth remembered, now, that pathetic cry from the heart, and he felt much ashamed that he had not attended to the matter ere this. But he had not forgotten this dear little woman, and, had they not met tonight, she would have heard from him within a day or two.

All at once, by what was a real accident, his fingers touched her bare arm. They lay on her soft flesh for the fraction of a minute, and it was as if she could feel the thrill which ran through him.

She did not move, she scarcely breathed. Neither could have said how long it was before those hard, cool fingers slid down and grasped her soft hand. He crushed her hand in his strong grasp, then let it go.

===Chinese===
“一个游手好闲的男人，”她当时这样说，声音颤抖而喑哑，几乎回回都能扯动听者的心弦，“钱想不花也由不得他。只要能替我丈夫寻到一份差事，叫我拿什么去换都甘愿！”

此刻，迈尔斯·拉什沃思想起了那一声发自肺腑的哀告，深愧自己竟拖到今日还不曾把这件事放在心上。可他并没有忘掉这位亲爱的小女人；倘若今晚不是这般巧遇，再过一两天，她也就会得到他的音信了。

冷不防地——纯属一场真正的意外——他的手指碰到了她裸露的手臂。手指在她柔软的肌肤上停留了不足一眨眼的工夫，而那一阵掠过他全身的震颤，她竟仿佛也能感同身受。

她一动也不动，连呼吸都几乎屏住了。过了多久，那几根坚硬微凉的手指才滑落下来，攥住她柔软的手，两人谁也说不清。他用有力的手掌紧紧一握，随即放开。

===Original===
“I suppose you would like Mr. Lexton to start work this autumn?” he said at last. “There isn’t much doing during August and September.”

His voice sounded strangely caressing and possessive, even to himself. But he felt sure that Ivy, a “nice” woman, had no suspicion of how much he had been moved by that casual, unexpected touch.

Miles Rushworth told himself that he must mind his step, for this seductive little creature, God help him, was another man’s wife, and he “wasn’t that sort.” Neither, he would have staked his life on it, was she.

===Chinese===
“我料想，你巴不得莱克斯顿先生今年秋天就上工吧？”他终于开口，“八月和九月里，是办不成什么事的。”

他的嗓音听上去出奇地温存，还带着一股占有的意味，连他自己都听出来了。但他满心笃定：艾薇是个“正派”女子，绝想不到那无意之间的一触，竟叫他心神激荡到了这般地步。

迈尔斯·拉什沃思告诫自己，脚步务必放稳，因为这个勾人心魄的小可人儿——老天爷帮帮忙——终究是别人的妻子，而他“不是那路人”。至于她么，他敢拿性命打赌，也同样不是。

===Original===
And yet? Was it he?﻿—sensible, prudent, nay, where women were concerned, overcautious﻿—Miles Rushworth, or some tricksy, bold entity outside himself which uttered the words: “By the way, what are you doing next month? If you’re doing nothing in particular, I do wish you’d both join my yachting party. Lady Dale and her daughter are coming, together with two or three others.”

A look of real, almost childlike, joy and pleasure flashed into Ivy Lexton’s face and, once more, the man sitting so closely by her side felt shaken to the depths. Tenderness was now added to the feeling of passionate attraction of which he was already half uncomfortably, half exultantly, aware. How young she looked, how innocent﻿—now, at this moment, like a happy little girl.

“D’you really mean that?” she cried. “I’ve always longed to go yachting! But I’ve never even been in a yacht. Jervis is awfully fond of the sea, too; he was at Cowes when the war broke out!”

===Chinese===
然而？方才说这话的，究竟是他——这位明白事理、谨小慎微、一涉及女人甚至宁可过分小心的迈尔斯·拉什沃思——还是什么蛰居在他躯壳之外、狡黠而大胆的精怪？“顺便问一句，下个月你们有什么打算？要是没有特别的事，我诚心盼着你们两位都来参加我的游艇聚会。戴尔夫人和她的女儿都来，另外还有两三位客人。”

艾薇·莱克斯顿的脸上倏地闪过一种真切的、近乎孩子气的欢欣；紧挨着她坐的这个人，又一次被震彻了肺腑。在那股他已经半是不安、半是踌躇满志地意识到的炽烈吸引之上，此刻又添了一重柔情。她看上去多么年轻，多么天真——此时此刻，活像个快活的小姑娘。

“你当真这么想？”她喊道，“我一直巴望着去坐游艇呢！可我连游艇都没上去过。杰维斯也顶喜欢海；开战那会儿，他正在考斯（Cowes）呢！”

===Original===
“Then that settles it,” exclaimed Rushworth delightedly. “We join the *Dark Lady* at Southampton on August the 5th! By the way, perhaps I ought to tell you that we’re not going on any specially wonderful trip. We’re only going to cruise about the coast of France. I’m afraid Lady Dale and her daughter will have to leave us fairly soon﻿—they’ve promised to stay with some people near Dieppe.”

“It will be heavenly﻿—heavenly!”

Ivy whispered those five words almost in his ear, for she was exceedingly anxious that Roger Gretorex should hear nothing of this delightful plan. She had promised the young man she would spend a week, during August, alone with him and his mother in the Sussex manor house which was still his own, though all the land up to the park gates had been sold.

===Chinese===
“那就一言为定！”拉什沃思喜形于色地喊道，“八月五日，我们在南安普敦（Southampton）登上‘黑夫人号’（Dark Lady）！对了，兴许我该告诉你，我们这趟并不是去航行什么特别了不得的路线，只沿着法国海岸一带巡游。戴尔夫人和她女儿恐怕住不了几天就得告辞——她们已经答应了到迪耶普（Dieppe）附近一些人家里做客。”

“那可太美了——太美了！”

这句五个词的话，艾薇几乎是贴着他的耳朵说的，因为她焦灼万分，生怕罗杰·格雷托雷克斯把这个美妙计划听去半点风声。她已经应承过这个年轻人：八月里，她要同他和他的母亲一起，在那座萨塞克斯（Sussex）庄园宅邸里单独住上一个星期——宅子至今仍归他名下，只是通到庄园大门为止的土地，都已卖光了。

===Original===
As she gave a quick surreptitious glance at the host who was her dangerously jealous lover﻿—even jealous, grotesque thought, of her husband, entirely unsuspicious Jervis﻿—a feeling of sharp irritation again swept over Ivy Lexton.

She told herself angrily that, though Roger Gretorex might belong by birth to grand people (to her surprise he made no effort to keep up with them), he had never been taught to behave as a young man should always behave in pleasant company. Even now, he still had what Ivy called “his thundercloud face,” and he was scarcely paying any attention to the girl sitting by him.

Ivy, not for the first time, realised that she had been a fool indeed to allow herself to become attracted to a man who was so little of her own sort. And yet Gretorex had been such a wonderful wooer! And his ardour had moved and excited her all the more because, at times, he had been as if overwhelmed with what had seemed to her an absurd kind of remorse at the knowledge that the woman he loved was another man’s wife.

===Chinese===
她飞快地偷觑了一眼做东的那位——她这位醋意十足的危险情人，竟然连她的丈夫、毫无猜疑的杰维斯也嫉妒，这念头未免荒唐——一阵尖锐的恼意又一次涌上艾薇·莱克斯顿的心头。

她愤愤地想：罗杰·格雷托雷克斯论出身固然属于显贵人家（令她诧异的是，他竟无意同这些人应酬往来），却从没学过年轻人置身于愉快场合所应有的那份规矩做派。直到这会儿，他脸上还挂着艾薇所谓的“那副雷雨云面孔”，对坐在身边的姑娘几乎全不理会。

艾薇并非头一回意识到：自己竟容许这样一个同她格格不入的男人把自己迷住，实在是傻透了。可是话说回来，格雷托雷克斯又曾是多么出色的一位求爱者！他的热切一度令她意动神摇、心旌激荡，尤其是因为，他时不时会被一种她看来荒唐透顶的悔恨压倒——只因他明白，自己爱的这个女人是别人的妻子。

===Original===
Dismissing the distasteful thought of Gretorex from her mind, she turned to Rushworth.

“Don’t say anything to my husband about this delightful plan,” she murmured. “I shall have to bring him round to the idea. You see, he’s so awfully eager to start work at once.”

The lights were now being turned off one by one, so Ivy smiled across at Lady Dale, and rose from the chair which touched that on which Rushworth was still sitting as if lost in a dream.

===Chinese===
她把格雷托雷克斯这桩败兴的念头从心里撵了出去，转向拉什沃思。

“这个美妙的计划，可别在我丈夫跟前露出半点口风，”她低声说，“我还得慢慢把他说通。你知道，他是一万个急着马上上工呢。”

餐厅的灯此刻正一盏接一盏地熄掉，于是艾薇朝戴尔夫人含笑示意，从椅子里站起身来——她那把椅子还挨着拉什沃思坐的那把，而他仍坐在那里，仿佛沉浸在一场梦中。

===Original===
As, a few moments later, they all stood together outside in the cool night air﻿—all, that is, but Roger Gretorex who, after having uttered a curt good night, had gone back to the now fast-emptying restaurant to pay his bill﻿—Miles Rushworth exclaimed: “We can all squeeze into my car, or, if not, I’ll go outside.”

Ivy was delighted. She very much disliked the spending of any unnecessary ready money just now; and the thought of going home in a crowded omnibus on this fine July night had been unbearable.

In the end it was Jervis Lexton who sat outside by the chauffeur, while inside the car the other four discussed their coming yachting tour.

At last the Rolls-Royce drew up before the shabby-looking, stucco-fronted house in Pimlico, and Rushworth helped Ivy Lexton out of his car with a strong, careful hand.

“Don’t ring,” she said hurriedly; “Jervis has a latchkey. This house belongs to an old servant of the Lexton family; that’s why we are living here.”

As Ivy’s husband opened the door, Ivy’s new friend caught a glimpse of the dirty, gaslit hall, and his heart swelled with mingled disgust and pity. He must get this sweet, dainty little woman out of this horrible place at once﻿—at once. Taking her hand in his, he held it just a thought longer than is perhaps usual even when a man is bidding good night to an exceptionally pretty woman.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，众人都已一同站在外面清凉的夜气里——所谓众人，独独除了罗杰·格雷托雷克斯：他草草道过一声晚安，便回那家此刻正迅速空下去的餐馆结账去了——迈尔斯·拉什沃思朗声说道：“都挤进我的车里去，挤不下的话，我就坐外座。”

艾薇满心欢喜。眼下任何一笔不必要的现钱开销，她都深恶痛绝；而在这个晴好的七月夜里，一想到要挤在公共汽车里回家，她简直无法忍受。

到头来，是杰维斯·莱克斯顿坐在外座司机旁边；车厢里其余四个人，则畅谈起即将来临的游艇之旅。

终于，劳斯莱斯（Rolls-Royce）在一幢外表寒伧、正面抹着灰泥的房子跟前停了下来——房子坐落在皮姆利科——拉什沃思伸出一只有力而稳当的手，扶艾薇·莱克斯顿下了车。

“别按铃，”她急急地说，“杰维斯带着开门的钥匙呢。这房子是莱克斯顿家一位老仆人的，所以我们才住在这儿。”

艾薇的丈夫推开门时，艾薇的新朋友一眼瞥见那煤气灯照着的、脏兮兮的门厅，心里登时涨满了嫌恶与怜悯交织的滋味。必须马上把这可爱娇俏的小女人从这可怕的地方弄出去——马上。他执起她的一只手握着，握的时候比寻常稍稍长了一点——哪怕一个男人向一位格外美丽的女人道晚安，也不见得握这么久。

===Original===
---

Long, long after Jervis Lexton was fast asleep in his crowded little back room, Ivy lay awake on the hard, lumpy, small double bed which took up most of the space in the front room.

She was tired, and with fatigue had come a feeling of depression. Miles Rushworth had said nothing as to their next meeting. He had forgotten her before﻿—he might forget her again. As for Mrs. Thrawn﻿—all that woman had told her might be fudge. The hard, shrewd side of Ivy’s nature came uppermost, and whispered that she had probably been very silly to spend a pound on a fortune-teller, and sillier still to believe in her predictions.

As she lay there, moving restlessly about, for it was a hot night, there came over her a feeling of revolt, almost of despair, at the conditions of her present day-to-day life. She was vividly aware of her own beauty﻿—what beautiful young woman is not? In a certain set, the world of the smart night clubs, she was known as “the lovely Mrs. Lexton.” Further, she was popular, well liked by all sorts of people, women as well as men, and dowered by nature with a keen appreciation of all that makes civilised life decorous, orderly, and attractive.

===Chinese===
---

杰维斯·莱克斯顿在拥挤的小后屋里睡熟之后许久许久，艾薇还睁着眼睛，躺在那张又硬又坑洼不平的小双人床上——这张床占去了前屋的大半地盘。

她累了，而疲惫又随之带来一阵消沉。迈尔斯·拉什沃思对下一次见面的事只字未提。他从前就把她忘掉过——往后保不齐还会再忘。至于索恩太太——那女人讲给她听的一套，兴许全是胡编。艾薇天性里冷酷精明的那一面浮了上来，悄声说：花一英镑去请教一个算命的女人，多半已经够傻的了；居然还信她的预言，那就更傻。

她躺在那里翻来覆去，因为这夜天气闷热；一想到眼前这种一日挨一日的光景，一阵愤懑、近乎绝望的情绪便攫住了她。她对自己美貌的分量再清楚不过——年轻貌美的女子，又有哪个不清楚呢？在某一个圈子里，就是那时髦夜总会的天地，她是有名的“美丽的莱克斯顿太太”。况且她人缘极好，男女各色人等都喜欢她；造物又赋予她一种敏锐的鉴赏力，凡使文明生活显得端庄、井然、动人的一切，她无不心领神会。

===Original===
Unlike some of her friends, she hated and despised Bohemian ways. She had tasted something of what Bohemia can offer her subjects during the few weeks she had spent in the chorus of a musical comedy. Yet now she was condemned﻿—she sincerely believed through no fault of her own﻿—to lead an existence full of sordid shifts, and of expedients so ignoble that even she sometimes shrank from them, while always on her slender shoulders lay the dead weight of her husband, a completely idle, extravagant, and yes, well she knew it, very stupid young man.

With angry distress she now asked herself a question of immediate moment. How was she to procure even the very simplest clothes suitable for life on a yacht? For a long time, now, she had had to pay ready money where she had once been welcome to unlimited credit.

Then in the darkness her face lightened. She had remembered Roger Gretorex! Poor though he was, he could always find money for her at a pinch. He had done so this very morning, and would of course do so again.

Then her face shadowed. Though Roger had his uses, he was becoming a tiresome, even a dangerous, complication in her life. Yet had it not been for him, had he not taken them to the Savoy tonight, she might never again have seen the man on whom now all her hopes centred.

===Chinese===
与她的某些朋友不同，她对波希米亚式的生活做派又恨又鄙。她曾在一场歌舞喜剧的合唱队里待过几个星期，多少尝过些波希米亚能款待其子民的滋味。然而如今，她却命定——她真心相信这绝非她自己的过错——要过一种充满腌臜的腾挪、充满不体面的穷对付的日子；那些应急的招数如此下作，连她自己有时也要畏缩；而她那单薄的肩膀上，永远压着丈夫这具死沉的累赘——一个彻头彻尾游手好闲、挥霍无度，而且，是的，她自己心里雪亮，蠢得可以的年轻男人。

她又气又苦，盘问自己一个火烧眉毛的问题：就连游艇生活所需的最简单的行头，她又从哪儿弄去？好久以来，那些从前欢迎她无限赊账的地方，如今都非付现钱不可了。

想着想着，黑暗中她的脸色开朗起来。她想起了罗杰·格雷托雷克斯！别看他穷，急难时节，他总归替她张罗得出钱来。就在今天早上，他还这样做了呢；往后当然还会再这样。

跟着，她的脸色又阴了下去。罗杰尽管有他的用处，却正在变成她生活里一桩恼人的、甚至危险的纠葛。然而，若不是有他，若不是他今晚带他们上了萨伏伊，她兴许就再也见不着那个如今寄托着她全部指望的男人了。

===Original===
Ivy Lexton had an intimate knowledge of the ugly, sinister sides of human nature. Her own father, a big man of business, had failed when she was seventeen. He had killed himself to avoid legal proceedings which would have led to a term of imprisonment. Their large circle of acquaintances (of real friends they had none) were some kind, some cruel, to the feckless, foolish, still pretty widow, and her lovely young daughter. The widow had soon married again, to die within a year. Ivy, after drifting about rudderless for a while, had obtained the “walking-on” part which had introduced her to an idle, pleasure-seeking, rich class of young men. By the time she was twenty she could have married half a dozen times. Her choice finally fell on Jervis Lexton, partly because he was of a superior social world to the other men who made love to her, but far more because at that time he had been undisputed owner of what had seemed to her a large fortune. Yet that fortune had melted like snow, lasting the two of them barely six years.﻿ ﻿…

Tossing about in her hot bed, Ivy reminded herself with a dawning feeling of hope, almost of security, that dull Lady Flora, who was no gossip, had said, during the weekend they had first met, that Miles Rushworth’s income was over a hundred thousand a year.﻿ ﻿…

As she was drinking her cup of tea the next morning, there was brought up to her an envelope, marked “Personal,” which had come by hand.

Eagerly tearing it open, at once she saw that, in addition to a letter, it contained a small plain envelope:

===Chinese===
艾薇·莱克斯顿对人性中丑恶、阴狠的一面，有着切身的了解。她自己的父亲——商界一位大人物——在她十七岁那年破了产；为了躲开一场终将招致牢狱之灾的官司，他自寻了短见。他们家那个庞大的熟人圈子（真正的朋友是一个也没有）对待这对没了主意、荒唐糊涂、风韵犹存的寡母和她可爱的年轻女儿，有的厚道，有的刻薄。寡母不久便改嫁了，不出一年就死了。艾薇无桨无舵地漂泊了一阵之后，寻到了一个“龙套角色”（walking-on part）；也正是这个角色，把她引进了那个游手好闲、寻欢作乐的富家子弟的圈子。到二十岁上，她本已可以嫁过六回了。她最终选中杰维斯·莱克斯顿，一半是因为在追求她的男人当中，他的社会门第高出一等；而更多的，是因为那时节，在他名下——据她看来——稳稳坐着一份大家业。谁知那份家业竟像雪一样化了，供养他们两口子，勉强撑了不到六年。……

艾薇在热得难受的床上辗转反侧，提醒自己——心里渐生出一股希望，几乎是一股安稳之感——那位言语无味的弗洛拉小姐——她可不是个爱传闲话的人——在他们初逢的那个周末说过，迈尔斯·拉什沃思的年收入在十万英镑开外。……

第二天早晨，她正喝着那杯茶，有人给她送上来一只信封，封皮上标着“亲启”，是专差送来的。

她急急地撕开封皮，一眼便看见里头除了一封信，还套着一只小小的素净信封：

===Original===
> My dear Mrs. Lexton,

> I have already thought of a job for your husband, but the earliest moment he can begin work would be the third week in September, say a week after our return from our yachting trip. This being so, I hope you will forgive me for sending you the enclosed cheque for a hundred pounds, which he can pay me back at his convenience after he has begun to draw his salary.

> I shall be so pleased if you and he will lunch with me tomorrow at the Carlton Grill. We can then make our final arrangements as to meeting at Southampton on August 5th.

> Yours very sincerely,

> Miles Rushworth.

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的莱克斯顿太太：

> 你丈夫的差事，我已经想妥了一个，只是最早也得等到九月的第三个星期才能上工，就算作我们游艇归来之后一个星期吧。既然如此，随函奉上一张一百英镑的支票，还望你海涵；等他开始支了薪水，方便的时候再还我不迟。

> 明天你若肯同他一起来卡尔顿烧烤餐厅（Carlton Grill）与我共进午餐，我将非常欢喜。届时我们可以把八月五日在南安普敦会合的最后一应安排定妥。

> 你非常真挚的，

> 迈尔斯·拉什沃思

===Original===
As Ivy drew out of the smaller envelope an uncrossed cheque made out to “self,” and endorsed “Miles Rushworth,” tears of joy rose to her eyes.

She ran into the next room, and excitedly told her husband the good news. But she said that the cheque their generous new friend had sent them “on account” was for fifty pounds.

Jervis Lexton leapt out of bed. “How splendid!” he exclaimed. And then, seizing her in his arms, he pirouetted in the tiny space left in the middle of the garret. “You are the cleverest as well as the prettiest little woman in the whole world!” he cried.

===Chinese===
艾薇从那只小信封里抽出一张未划线的支票，抬头开着“self”（本人），背面签有“迈尔斯·拉什沃思”的背书；喜悦的泪水顿时涌上了她的眼睛。

她跑进隔壁房间，把这个好消息兴冲冲地告诉了丈夫。只是她说，他们那位慷慨的新朋友送来的这张“暂记垫付”的支票，是五十英镑。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿从床上一跃而起。“太好啦！”他欢呼道。接着，他一把将她搂进怀中，在阁楼当中仅剩的一小块空地上打起旋来。“你是全世界最聪明、也是最漂亮的小妻子！”他喊道。

## III / 三
===Original===
“Do look at Mrs. Lexton! Isn’t she absolutely lovely, Miles?”
“Yes, Bella﻿—and as good as she is pretty, I really do believe,” was the half-joking answer.
Look at her? Rushworth had done very little else since Ivy had come out of her stateroom this morning.
The two speakers were standing on the deck of the *Dark Lady*, and three yards away Ivy Lexton, lying back in a deck-chair, was talking animatedly to one of her fellow-guests, a good-looking young man named Quirk, who after having done well in the war, had been very nearly down and out by 1921, when he had been found and succoured by Rushworth. He now had his own plane, his air-taxi as he called it, and, thanks again to Rushworth, he never lacked good customers.

===Chinese===
“快看莱克斯顿太太！她是不是美极了，迈尔斯？”

“是啊，贝拉（Bella）——而且我真心相信，她人有多美，心肠就有多好。”回答里带着几分玩笑。

看她？打从艾薇今天早上走出舱房起，拉什沃思做的差不多就只有这一件事。

说话的两人正站在黑夫人号（*Dark Lady*）的甲板上；三码开外，艾薇·莱克斯顿半躺在一张甲板躺椅里，正同船上的另一位客人谈得起劲。那是位相貌英俊的年轻人，名叫奎克（Quirk）：战争中他颇有建树，到 1921 年却已潦倒得几乎不名一文，是拉什沃思发现了他、拉了他一把。如今他有了自己的飞机——他管它叫他的“空中出租车”——而也多亏拉什沃思，他的好主顾从没断过。

===Original===
It was true that Mrs. Lexton looked lovely today. All the lovelier because she was thoroughly enjoying her new role, that of a perfectly turned out yachtswoman.
But Miles Rushworth had already told himself more than once, in the last hour, that he would be cool, detached, impartial, when considering this special guest.
“I suppose you couldn’t say a word to her, just pointing out that she’s quite pretty enough to do without lipstick and rouge? I wish you’d tell her they don’t look, somehow, the right thing on a yacht.”
Bella Dale smiled and shook her head. “If you want me to make friends with her, that would be a very poor beginning﻿—”

===Chinese===
莱克斯顿太太今天看上去确实动人。而她正畅快地受用着新角色——一位装扮得尽善尽美的游艇女客——这就愈发显得动人了。

可就在方才这一个钟头里，迈尔斯·拉什沃思已经不止一次告诫自己：对这位特殊的客人，他要冷静、超然、不偏不倚。

“你能不能跟她提一句，就说她天生丽质，不涂唇膏胭脂也够漂亮了？我倒希望你能告诉她，那些东西搁在游艇上，不知怎么总显得不像样。”

贝拉·戴尔（Bella Dale）莞尔摇头。“你要是想让我跟她交朋友，拿这个开场可就太糟了——”

===Original===
He said suddenly, “I am afraid Lady Dale doesn’t care for Mrs. Lexton?”
The colour deepened in his companion’s cheeks, and she looked embarrassed.
“Mother hasn’t had much of a chance of talking to her yet.”
Bella Dale was uncomfortably aware that her mother had taken an instant dislike to Ivy Lexton on the evening they had first met at the Savoy; and she knew that Lady Dale’s feeling had increased, rather than lessened, since the Lextons had joined Miles Rushworth’s yacht, for she had exclaimed to her daughter in the privacy of their stateroom: “It’s foolish to be too good-natured, Bella. That young woman is a regular little minx!”

===Chinese===
他突然说：“我看，戴尔夫人（Lady Dale）是不喜欢莱克斯顿太太吧？”

他身旁这位同伴颊上的红晕更深了，神情颇为窘迫。

“妈妈还没有多少机会同她说话呢。”

贝拉·戴尔心里不安地明白，母女俩头一回在萨伏伊饭店（the Savoy）见到艾薇·莱克斯顿的那个晚上，母亲就当即对她生出了反感；她也知道，自打莱克斯顿夫妇登上迈尔斯·拉什沃思的游艇，戴尔夫人的这份情绪有增无减——因为她曾背着人、在母女俩的舱房里对女儿感叹：“心肠太软是愚蠢的，贝拉。那个年轻女人是个十足的小狐狸精！”

===Original===
But Bella Dale, at this time of her life, saw everything through Miles Rushworth’s eyes. She liked what he liked, admired what he admired, and at any rate tried to believe good what he believed good. He had asked her earnestly to make friends with Mrs. Lexton, and he had told her something of the struggle the poor, pretty, little thing had gone through. Also he had let her see how great was his contempt for Ivy’s worthless, extravagant, idle husband.﻿ ﻿…
Rushworth had always had from childhood a passion for the sea. His had been an old-fashioned home, and everything had been done by his parents to promote what they thought was for his happiness from the day he was born; but not once had he been asked what he wished to do in life. His path had been marked out for him almost, it may be said, before his birth. His father would have been surprised as well as dismayed to learn that, both as a child and as a youth, his great wish had been to enter the Navy. During the war he had given to naval charities what would have crippled a lesser fortune than his own.
