His fine yacht was his one personal extravagance, and on the *Dark Lady* he spent by far the happiest hours of his life. But he had deliberately so arranged the accommodation that it was impossible for him to have a really big party aboard. Eight to ten, including himself, was his limit, and the same people were generally asked by him each year. Lady Dale and her daughter, together with an old-fashioned couple belonging to a rather older generation than himself, who looked forward the whole year through to this August yachting fortnight, always came. To these he had added this summer the flying man, the latter’s bride, and the Lextons.
Acting as hostess was a middle-aged spinster cousin of his mother’s, who, like himself, had a passion for the sea. Charlotte Chattle was a pleasant woman of the world, speaking both French and Italian well, and clever in organising expeditions for those of his guests who cared for land jaunts. But the only people who counted in Rushworth’s mind on this summer cruise were Lady Dale and her daughter, and Ivy Lexton and Ivy Lexton’s husband.

===Chinese===
可是贝拉·戴尔在她一生的这个时期，看什么都用迈尔斯·拉什沃思的眼光。他喜欢的她就喜欢，他赞赏的她就赞赏，至少也竭力相信，凡他信为善的便是善。他恳切地请她与莱克斯顿太太交朋友，也对她讲了这个可怜、漂亮的小东西经历过的种种挣扎。他还让她看出，他对艾薇那个没出息、挥霍、游手好闲的丈夫是何等鄙夷。……

拉什沃思从小就对大海怀有一份痴情。他出身于一个守旧的家庭，从落生那天起，父母便事事为他张罗，一心促成他们所以为的他的幸福；可从来没有一个人问过他，这辈子想做什么。他的路，几乎可以说，早在出生之前就替他铺好了。倘若父亲得知——无论他儿时还是少年时代——他最大的心愿就是进海军，准会又惊又痛。大战期间，他捐给海军慈善事业的款项，若是换一份不及他家雄厚的产业，早叫它伤了元气。

那艘华丽的游艇是他唯一任性的私己挥霍，而在黑夫人号上，他度过了一生中最快活的时光。不过他刻意把舱位安排得再也无法容纳一大帮客人。连他自己在内，八到十人是极限，而且每年请的通常是同一批人。戴尔夫人和她的女儿总要来，还有一对守旧的老夫妇——比他年长一辈，一年到头就盼着八月里这趟游艇双周之游。今年夏天，他又添请了那位飞行家、飞行家的新娘，以及莱克斯顿夫妇。

充当女主人的，是他母亲的一位中年表亲，一位老小姐，同他一样痴心爱海。夏洛特·查特尔（Charlotte Chattle）是位讨人喜欢的、见过世面的女人，法语意大利语都说得好，又擅长为那些爱上岸游逛的客人张罗短途出行。可是在拉什沃思心里，这次夏季巡航中真正要紧的，只有戴尔夫人和她的女儿，以及艾薇·莱克斯顿和艾薇·莱克斯顿的丈夫。

===Original===
Ivy’s half-presentiment at the Savoy had been perhaps a case of thought transference, for Miles Rushworth, just about that time, had been thinking seriously of marrying Bella Dale. Indeed, had that meeting with the Lextons not taken place, he would almost certainly have been engaged by now to Bella, and he still so far deceived himself as to wish that the girl he thought he loved, and whom he intended to become Mrs. Miles Rushworth, should make friends with Ivy Lexton.
Bella Dale had done her best in the last three days to fall in with his wishes, but she found it difficult to get further than a mild acquaintanceship with Miles Rushworth’s beautiful guest. She knew nothing of the night club, dancing, racing life, which was all that both the Lextons knew and thought worth living for. And Ivy, on her side, was entirely ignorant of, and would have despised, had she known of them, the manifold social and general interests which filled the life of even so quiet a girl as Bella Dale. Also Bella, who was no fool, realised with some discomfort that Mrs. Lexton had very quickly become aware that Lady Dale did not like or approve of her.
And Ivy herself? Ivy was counting the hours﻿—to her intense relief they had now become hours instead of days﻿—to the time when Lady Dale and her daughter would leave the yacht at Dieppe.
During the three weeks that had elapsed since their memorable meeting at the Savoy, Ivy Lexton and Miles Rushworth had been constantly together. It had all been very much above board﻿—indeed, quite as often as not, Jervis Lexton had been of the company when the two lunched or dined, went to the play, or, pleasanter still, motored down to Ranelagh to spend an enchanting evening.

===Chinese===
艾薇在萨伏伊饭店时那似有若无的预感，说不定正是一桩心灵感应（thought transference）——因为差不多就在那个时候，迈尔斯·拉什沃思正认真地考虑娶贝拉·戴尔。的确，倘若没有同莱克斯顿夫妇的那次相遇，他如今几乎肯定已经同贝拉订了婚；而他至今仍在自欺，巴望这位他自以为爱着、并打算让她成为迈尔斯·拉什沃思太太的姑娘，能同艾薇·莱克斯顿结为朋友。

这三天里，贝拉·戴尔竭力顺着他的心意去做，可她发现，同迈尔斯·拉什沃思这位美丽的客人之间，实在难以越过淡淡的一面之缘再往前一步。夜总会、跳舞、赛马的那一套生活，她一无所知——而那正是莱克斯顿夫妇全部的学问、也是他们认为活着才值得的事。反过来，充实着贝拉·戴尔——哪怕像她这么娴静的姑娘——一生的种种社交与各方趣尚，艾薇也全然无知；就算知道了，也会嗤之以鼻。再者，贝拉并不是傻子，她多少有些不自在地意识到：莱克斯顿太太早已飞快地看出，戴尔夫人不喜欢她、也不认可她。

那么艾薇自己呢？艾薇正掰着钟点数——叫她大大宽慰的是，如今数的是钟点而非日子——数到戴尔夫人母女在迪耶普（Dieppe）下船的时刻。

自从在萨伏伊饭店那次难忘的相逢，三个星期过去了，艾薇·莱克斯顿和迈尔斯·拉什沃思时时作伴。一切都摆在了明面上——的确，两人用午餐、吃晚饭、看戏，或者更惬意的，驱车去拉尼勒（Ranelagh）消磨一个迷人夜晚的时候，杰维斯·莱克斯顿倒也往往在场。

===Original===
But Rushworth had a definite philosophy of life. To pursue a woman who, whatever the undercurrents to her life might be, appeared happily married, would have seemed to him a despicable, as well as a cruel and unmanly thing to do. Also, he prided himself on being able, when he chose to do so, to resist temptation, and he felt convinced he could handle what might become a delicate situation not only with sense, but even with comfort to himself. This was made the easier to him because he put Ivy Lexton on a pedestal. God alone knew how he idealised her, how completely he believed her soul matched her delicately perfect, ethereal-looking body.
While Ivy was chatting gaily to her companion, she was yet almost painfully aware of the two who stood talking together in so earnest and intimate a way. She was feeling what she had never felt in her life of twenty-six years: that is, bitterly, angrily jealous of a girl whom she thought stupid, dull, and unattractive.
Miles Rushworth’s attitude to herself disconcerted her. She could not, to use her own jargon, get the hang of him. It was so strange, in a sense so disturbing, that he never made love to her. Then, now and again, she would remember Mrs. Thrawn, and Mrs. Thrawn’s predictions.
She had followed the fortune-teller’s advice with regard to Roger Gretorex. She had insisted that it would be better for them both neither to see nor to write to each other till she came back to London in September; and he had had perforce to agree to her conditions.

===Chinese===
可是拉什沃思自有一套明确的人生哲学。一个女子，无论她生活的暗流如何，看上去婚姻美满，去追这样的女人，在他看来既是卑劣之举，也是残忍而有失丈夫气概的行径。再者，他颇为自负的一点是：只要他愿意，就能抵御诱惑；他深信，这个或许会变得微妙的局面，他不仅能应付得有分寸，甚至还能应付得安然自得。他能如此，还因为他把艾薇·莱克斯顿供在了神龛里。只有上帝知道他把她理想化到了什么地步，知道他何等完完全全地相信，她的灵魂配得上她那纤丽完美、飘飘若仙的身姿。

艾薇一面兴冲冲地同同伴闲谈，一面却近乎痛苦地觉察着那并肩而立、谈得那样恳切而亲昵的两个人。她正体验着二十六年生命中从未体验过的滋味：对一个她认为愚笨、乏味、毫无吸引力的姑娘，生出刻毒而怨愤的嫉妒。

迈尔斯·拉什沃思对她的态度弄得她心神不宁。用她自己的行话来说，她“摸不透”他这个人。他从来不向她求爱，这未免蹊跷，在某种意义上还令人不安。于是，她时不时会想起索恩太太（Mrs. Thrawn），想起索恩太太的种种预言。

对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯（Roger Gretorex），她听从了算命女人的忠告。她执意主张，两人既不见面也不通信，直到她九月回伦敦为止，这样对彼此都好；他也只得被迫应承了她的条件。

---

===Original===
The yacht made Dieppe the next morning, and at breakfast there rose a discussion as to how the party could spend their time on shore to the best advantage. Rushworth at once observed that he would not be able to take part in any expedition ashore. He had received important business telegrams, and he had a number of letters to dictate to a stenographer whose services he had already secured.
Miss Chattle, who knew he would value a quiet working day, suggested a motor expedition to a celebrated shrine a hundred kilometres inland from Dieppe. She declared that if they started at once they could be back in comfortable time for dinner.
And then it was that Ivy, as in a lightning flash, made up her mind as to how she would spend today.
“I get so tired motoring, so I’d rather stay behind.” She turned to her host, “While you’re doing your work, I can take a walk in the town. Though I’ve been to Paris two or three times, I’ve never been anywhere else in France.”

===Chinese===
第二天上午，游艇抵了迪耶普。早餐桌上起了一场讨论：一行人怎样上岸，才能把时间花得最值。拉什沃思当即声明，任何上岸远游他都参加不了。他收到了要紧的业务电报，还有一沓信要口授给一位速记员——此人的差事他已经安排妥了。

查特尔小姐知道他看重一个清静的工作日，便提议乘汽车往内陆远游——去迪耶普以里一百公里的一处著名圣地。她断言，只要即刻动身，晚饭前从容赶回不成问题。

就在这当口，艾薇灵光一闪，打定了今天该怎么过的主意。

“坐汽车我总坐得腻，宁可留在船上。”她转向主人，“你办公的时候，我可以到城里散散步。巴黎我倒是去过两三回，法国别的地方还哪儿都没去过呢。”

===Original===
“That’s a good idea! We might meet at the Hotel Royal about one o’clock, and have lunch together.”
Half an hour later Miss Chattle shepherded the rest of the party into two roomy cars, while Rushworth escorted Lady Dale and her daughter on to the quay, where a carriage was waiting for them.
Lady Dale went forward to speak to the driver, and Rushworth turned to the girl he still intended should be his wife.
“If we don’t meet again before the end of September, I do want just to say one thing to you, Bella.”

===Chinese===
“这主意好！咱们可以一点钟左右在皇家饭店（Hotel Royal）碰头，一块儿吃午饭。”

半小时后，查特尔小姐照管着把其余客人送上两辆宽敞的汽车；拉什沃思则陪戴尔夫人母女登上码头，那里已经候着一辆马车。

戴尔夫人走上前去同车夫说话，拉什沃思便转向那位他仍旧打算娶为妻子的姑娘。

“要是九月底之前咱们见不上了，贝拉，我确实有一句话要对你说。”

===Original===
He spoke in so peculiar, and in so very earnest, a tone, that Bella’s heart began to beat.
“What is it you want to say?” she asked, her voice sinking almost to a whisper.
“I’ve said it before, and now I want to say it again﻿—”
Bella looked at him fixedly. Thank God, she hadn’t betrayed herself. But what was this he was saying?

===Chinese===
他的语气那样奇特、那样恳切，贝拉的心不禁怦怦跳了起来。

“你想说什么？”她问，声音低得近乎耳语。

“这话我从前说过，如今我想再说一遍——”

贝拉目不转睛地望着他。感谢上帝，她不曾泄露自己的心事。可他现在说的这是什么呢？

===Original===
“I do want you to make real friends with Mrs. Lexton﻿—I mean, of course, after you and Lady Dale are back at Hampton Court, when Jervis Lexton will have begun work in my London office. His wife, poor little soul, hasn’t any real friends, from what I can make out.”
“Yet she seems to know a good many people, Miles. When we were looking through those picture papers yesterday, she seemed to know almost everyone who had been snapshotted at Goodwood!”
“I was thinking of real friends﻿—not of those stupid gadabouts who are here, there, and everywhere,” he said with a touch of irritation.
And then they heard Lady Dale’s voice.

===Chinese===
“我是真心希望你和莱克斯顿太太结成真正的朋友——当然，我指的是等你和戴尔回到汉普顿宫（Hampton Court）、杰维斯·莱克斯顿到我伦敦的办事处上任之后。据我所知，他的妻子，那个可怜的小人儿，连一个真正的朋友都没有。”

“可她认识的人好像不少啊，迈尔斯。昨天我们翻那些画报的时候，在古德伍德（Goodwood）被偷拍进镜头的，她几乎个个都认得！”

“我说的是真正的朋友——不是那种东跑西颠、满天下的蠢货闲人。”他说，带着几分恼意。

就在这时，两人听见了戴尔夫人的声音。

===Original===
“I think we ought to be off, Bella. It’s nearly half-past ten, and you know they lunch early at the château.”
Rushworth wrung Bella’s hand. “I’m sorry you’ve had to leave the yacht so soon.”
But his voice had become perceptibly colder. He was disappointed, even a little hurt. He had always thought his friend Bella not only kind, but full of sympathy and understanding. Yet she had spoken of his new friend with a curious lack even of liking, let alone sympathy.

===Chinese===
“我看我们该动身了，贝拉。快十点半了，你知道的，城堡里午饭吃得早。”

拉什沃思紧紧握了握贝拉的手。“真遗憾，你们这么早就得离船。”

可他的嗓音分明冷了下来。他失望了，甚至有几分委屈。他一向以为他的朋友贝拉不但心地善良，而且极富同情、善解人意。可她谈起他的新朋友时，竟连一点好感都奇异地欠缺，更遑论同情了。

---

===Original===
When Miles Rushworth came back from seeing the Dales off, he found Ivy Lexton sitting on the now deserted deck. There was a pile of newspapers on the little table which had been brought up close to her deck-chair, and she was pretending to read the Paris *New York Herald*. Convinced that Miles Rushworth intended to be with her the whole of the long sunny morning, she was not only surprised, but also very disappointed, when he said cheerfully:
“Well, lovely lady, I’ve a hard mornin’s work before me, for there’s a whole pile of letters and telegrams waiting to be answered. Cook’s man has found me an excellent shorthand writer, so I hope to be through in a couple of hours.”
Her face suddenly became overcast, and he felt tempted, for a moment, to throw aside his work. But he resisted the temptation.

===Chinese===
迈尔斯·拉什沃思送走戴尔母女回来，发现艾薇·莱克斯顿坐在如今已空无一人的甲板上。躺椅近旁的小桌上摆着一叠报纸，她正装作在读巴黎版的《纽约先驱报》（*New York Herald*）。她原以为迈尔斯·拉什沃思打算陪她消磨这长长的晴朗一上午，所以听他兴冲冲地说出下面这句话时，她不单吃惊，还非常失望：

“好了，可爱的女士，我上午有件苦差事等着呢——整整一堆信件电报要回复。库克旅行社（Cook）的人给我找了位顶呱呱的速记员，我指望两个钟头就办完。”

她的脸色忽然阴了下来，他有一瞬间真想把手头的活儿撂开。但他抵住了这个诱惑。

===Original===
“Would you rather laze about here or take a walk and meet me at the Royal?”
“I’ll go into the town. There are one or two little things I want to buy. What time shall I be at the hotel?”
He hadn’t meant to meet her till one o’clock. But for once the old Adam triumphed.
“Let me see? It’s half-past ten now, let’s meet at twelve-thirty. We’ll have an early French lunch, and then we’ll go for a motor drive, or do anything else that you feel like doing. From what I can make out, the others can’t be back till seven, if then.”

===Chinese===
“你是愿意在这儿懒懒地待着，还是出去走走，到皇家饭店同我会合？”

“我进城去。有一两样小东西要买。我几点到饭店合适？”

他本打算一点钟再同她会面。可是这一回，他心底那个“老亚当”（the old Adam，喻与生俱来的原始冲动）占了上风。

“让我想想——现在十点半，那咱们就十二点半碰头。先早早地吃一顿法式午餐，然后开车兜风去，或者随你想做什么都行。据我看来，其他人不到七点回不来，能不能七点赶回还两说呢。”

===Original===
Ivy waited till she had seen him disappear into the stateroom which was the one retreat on the yacht where Rushworth never asked any of his guests to join him, and about which they all felt a certain curiosity. Then she put down the paper she still held in her hand, and, closing her eyes, she began to think.
What manner of man was this new friend of hers? He must “like” her surely? “Like” was the ambiguous term Ivy Lexton used to herself when she meant something very different from “liking.” Yet he had never said to her the sort of thing that the men she met almost always did say, and on the shortest acquaintance. Stranger still, he had never asked anything of her in exchange for what had become considerable and frequent benefactions. True, Rushworth’s gifts had almost always been useful gifts. He had never, so to speak, “said it with flowers.” That had puzzled her a little, made her sometimes wonder as to what his real feelings could be. Never once﻿—she had made a note of it in her own mind﻿—had he mentioned Bella Dale during the three weeks when they had been so much together in London.
So it had been a disagreeable shock to find Lady Dale and her daughter already established on the yacht, and on the happiest terms of old friendship with everyone on board. Again and again during the week’s cruise, Ivy had asked herself anxiously whether Miles Rushworth could really “like” such a dowdy, matter-of-fact girl as was Miss Dale? Yet now and again when she saw them together, talking in an intimate, happy way, and when she heard them alluding to events which had happened long before she knew Rushworth, there would come over her a tremor of icy fear, for well she knew that, from her point of view, a man friend married was a man friend marred.

===Chinese===
艾薇等着，直到望见他走进那间舱房——全游艇上唯一一处拉什沃思从不邀任何客人进去的幽居之所，大家对它都怀着几分好奇。随后她放下手里还捏着的报纸，闭上眼睛，思索起来。

她这位新朋友究竟是个什么样的人？他一定“喜欢”她吧？“喜欢”——这是艾薇·莱克斯顿对自己使用的一个含义暧昧的字眼，她的真意同“喜欢”相去远矣。然而他从不曾对她说那些她结识的男人几乎个个都会说的话，哪怕交情再浅也照说不误。更蹊跷的是，对于他已经给出的那些数目可观、源源不断的恩惠，他从不向她索取任何回报。不错，拉什沃思的馈赠几乎向来是实用的馈赠。可以说，他从来不曾“以花传情”（said it with flowers）。这一点让她有点纳闷，有时不免揣摩他真正的感情究竟是什么。还有——她在心里记下了——两人伦敦过从甚密的那三个星期里，他一次也没有提起过贝拉·戴尔。

因此，发现戴尔夫人母女早已在游艇上安顿如常，同船上人人处得像老朋友一般融洽，对她无异于一记败兴的闷棍。这一周的巡游里，艾薇一次又一次焦灼地自问：迈尔斯·拉什沃思当真会“喜欢”戴尔小姐这样一个衣饰寒素、刻板乏味的姑娘吗？然而时不时地，她瞧见他们俩在一起，亲昵而快活地谈着，听见他们提起那些早在她认识拉什沃思之前发生的事，一阵冰冷的战栗便会袭上心头——因为她太清楚了：在她看来，男朋友一旦结了婚，便算是毁了。

===Original===
It was to her a new experience to be in close touch with such a real worker as was Miles Rushworth. There was nothing in common between him and the idle, often vicious, and for the most part mindless young men who drifted in and out of the spendthrift world in which she and Jervis had both been so popular as long as their money had lasted.
She got up at last, and went into her luxurious stateroom to fetch a parasol. It was a charming costly trifle, matching the blue coat and skirt she was wearing, but large enough to shelter her face from the sun. Her quaint little sailor hat, a throwback to a mode of long ago, while very becoming, was quite useless from that point of view.
She walked slowly along the deck, hoping against hope that Rushworth would see her and, leaving his work, join her; but as she passed his stateroom she heard his voice dictating.

===Chinese===
同迈尔斯·拉什沃思这样一位真正的实干家朝夕相处，对她是一种全新的体验。他同那些游手好闲——往往还品行恶劣——而且多半没脑子的年轻男人毫无共通之处；那号人在她和杰维斯曾混迹的挥霍世界里进进出出，而当年只要钱还撑得住，两口子在那世界里都曾是风光的人物。

她终于起身，走进自己那间豪华的舱房去取阳伞。那是一件可爱而昂贵的随身小玩意儿，同她身上那套蓝色衣裙正相配，伞面却也够大，足以遮住她的脸挡住日头。她那顶古雅的小水手帽——旧日风尚的回光——虽然戴着很好看，论遮阳却全然不管用。

她沿甲板缓缓走去，明知无望仍存着一丝指望，盼拉什沃思能看见她、撂下工作出来陪她；可路过他的舱房时，她听见了他口授信件的声音。

---

===Original===
The French of all ages and both sexes are lovers of beauty, so in a small way Ivy Lexton’s progress through the picturesque old town of Dieppe was a triumphal progress. Most of the people she passed turned and looked after her with unaffected admiration and one man﻿—she felt instinctively that he was some important person﻿—followed her for quite a long way. But it was very hot, and in time she grew weary of the crowded streets. Taking as her guides a couple who were carrying their bathing costumes and towels, she went after them up a shady byway and so through the old gateway leading to the wide lawns along the seafront, which are the great charm of Dieppe.
What an amusing, lively, delightful place! Against the deep blue sky rose the white Casino, and the parking place was crowded with serried rows of motors. Along the front groups of Frenchwomen, for the most part wearing white coats and skirts, strolled about with their attendant cavaliers.
Her spirits bounded up; she felt herself to be once more what she had not felt herself to be at all on Rushworth’s yacht, in her own natural atmosphere again. And, to add to her satisfaction, she soon spied out the Hotel Royal, brilliant with flowers and blue and white sun-blinds.
The Angelus chimes rang out from one of the old churches, and the gay crowd began to move slowly towards the villas and hotels which form the seafront side of the incongruously-named Boulevard de Verdun.

===Chinese===
法国人不分男女老幼都爱美，所以艾薇·莱克斯顿行经迪耶普这座景色如画的古城，倒也小小地享受了一路凯旋。她走过之处，大多数人都要回头目送，毫不掩饰他们的倾慕；还有一个男人——她凭直觉感到是个什么要人——跟了她好长一程。只是天气实在炎热，渐渐地她便对拥挤的街道厌倦了。她瞅准一对带着泳衣和浴巾的夫妇作向导，跟着他们走上一条绿荫夹道的小巷，穿过古老的城门，来到海滨宽阔的草坪——那正是迪耶普最迷人之处。

多么有趣、多么生气勃勃、多么叫人愉快的地方！湛蓝的天空下耸立着白色的赌场（Casino），停车场上汽车排得密密层层。海滨大道上，一群群法国女人——多半穿着白色的衣裙外套——在各自殷勤的男伴陪伴下信步闲游。

她的兴致一下子高涨起来；在拉什沃思的游艇上她始终不曾有过的那种感觉——做回了自己——此刻又回来了，她重新置身于自己天然的氛围里。更叫她称心的是，她很快便望见了皇家饭店，鲜花同蓝白相间的遮阳篷映得它一派明艳。

一座老教堂里响起了三钟经的钟声（the Angelus），欢乐的人群开始缓缓向海滨一侧的别墅和旅馆移动——那排房子所临的马路，偏冠着“凡尔登大道”（Boulevard de Verdun）这么个不伦不类的名字。

===Original===
Ivy walked into the cool hall of the hotel, and sat down in an easy chair with a sigh of pleasure.
How she wished she was staying here instead of on the yacht! She delighted in the atmosphere of gay bustle and carefree wealth and prosperity of all the happy-looking people who were strolling past her on their way to the restaurant. She enjoyed the glances of covert, and in some cases of insolent, admiration thrown her way; in fact she was kept so well amused that she gave quite a start when she heard Rushworth’s voice exclaim, “So here you are, little lady! I’ve been looking for you everywhere. I thought you’d be out of doors.”
She got up, and then he said something which filled her with dismay.
“Among my letters this morning there was one from a very old friend of mine, a man with whom I worked during the war. He and his wife have a room in some back street, for they’re not at all well off. So I thought it would be a good plan to take them for a drive this afternoon. I felt sure you wouldn’t mind?”

===Chinese===
艾薇走进饭店凉快的门厅，在一把安乐椅里坐下，快慰地舒了口气。

她多想住在这里，而不是住在游艇上啊！她喜欢这欢快熙攘的气氛，喜欢那些打她身边走向餐厅、个个春风满面的人身上那股不虑明天的富足殷实之气。投向她的那些目光，有藏而不露的倾慕，有的干脆放肆大胆，她照单全收，受用得很；事实上她正自得其乐，猛听得拉什沃思一声招呼，倒吓了一跳：“原来你在这儿哪，小女士！我到处找你。我还当你在室外呢。”

她站起身来，这时他说了一句叫她满心懊丧的话。

“今天早上我的信里，有一封是一位很老的朋友写来的——战争期间我同他共过事。他和他太太住在某条后街的一间屋子里，日子一点也不宽裕。所以我想，今天下午带他们开车兜兜风倒是个好主意。你肯定不会介意吧？”

===Original===
“Of course not!”
She felt bitterly disappointed, but she would have been more than disappointed had she known that Rushworth had deliberately asked these old friends to join them, in order to put temptation out of his way.
He added, a little quickly, “I felt rather a brute not asking them to lunch, but I was so looking forward to my lunch alone with you.”
“I’d been looking forward to it, too,” she said in a low voice.

===Chinese===
“当然不介意！”

她心里失望得发苦；可她若知道，拉什沃思是特意请了这几位老朋友同行、好把诱惑从自己身边支开，那她就远不止是失望了。

他又急急地补了一句：“不请他们吃午饭，我觉得自己简直像个狠心的家伙；可我太盼着同你单独午餐了。”

“我也一直盼着呢。”她低声说。

接着，他当真感到一阵尖锐而无济于事的懊悔，悔自己方才蠢兮兮地讲什么骑士风度；他随即打定主意，兜风决不超过两个钟头。说到底，他和艾薇都是正派人，还是亲爱的朋友；旁人回来之前，他们为什么不能回游艇上去，安安静静、快快活活地单独待上一两个钟头呢？

===Original===
And then there did come across him a sharp, unavailing pang of regret that he had been so stupidly quixotic, and instantly he made up his mind that their drive should not last more than two hours. After all, he and Ivy were both decent people, and dear friends to boot; why shouldn’t they go back to the yacht to spend a quiet happy hour or two, alone together, before the others returned?
They had a delicious lunch, the sort of lunch that Ivy enjoyed, in an airy room full of chattering, merry, prosperous-looking couples.
Then, after they had had coffee, they went out and slowly sauntered to the little garden at the foot of a great cliff on which stands an ancient stronghold. It was cool and quiet there, and the only person with whom they shared the garden was an old lady exercising her Persian cat on a lead.
They sat down in silence. Rushworth was smoking a cigar, Ivy a cigarette. Suddenly he threw away his cigar, for there had come over him a wild, mad impulse to put his arms round her. But, instead, he moved a little farther away.

===Chinese===
他们吃了一顿可口的午餐——正是艾薇喜欢的那一种——在一间敞亮通风的房间里，四下里坐满了叽叽喳喳、喜气洋洋、看上去丰衣足食的一对对男女。

喝过咖啡，他们出门缓步徜徉，走到一座巍峨崖脚下的小花园，崖顶矗立着一座古老的堡垒。园子里清凉而幽静，同他们共享这园子的只有一位老太太，正牵着链子遛她的波斯猫。

两人默默坐下。拉什沃思抽着雪茄，艾薇抽着香烟。突然，他把雪茄扔掉了，因为一阵狂烈的冲动攫住了他，他想伸臂搂住她。然而他没有，反倒挪远了一点。

她也突然掐掉香烟，转向他：“拉什沃思先生，我有时候琢磨，你知不知道，你为我做的一切——还有为杰维斯做的——我有多么多么感激。”

===Original===
She, too, suddenly flung away her cigarette, and turned to him, “I sometimes wonder, Mr. Rushworth, if you know how awfully grateful I am to you for all you’ve done for me﻿—and for Jervis.”
He saw that tears were in her eyes, and he took her hand and clasped it closely. He was saying to himself, “Poor little darling, it would be the act of a cad, of a cur, to take advantage of her gratitude and﻿—and loneliness.”
“You’ve nothing to be grateful for,” he said quietly, and then he released the soft hand he held. “It’s a great privilege to meet someone who really deserves a little help. A man who is known to have money is there to be shot at,” he smiled a little grimly. “Any number of what are called deserving objects are presented to his view. The real problem is to find the people who want helping, and who won’t ask for help.”
He sincerely believed that the woman to whom he was addressing those words fell within that rare category.

===Chinese===
他看见她眼里含着泪，便握住她的手，紧紧攥着。他心里在说：“可怜的小亲亲，趁人之危利用她的感激，还有——还有她的孤单，那是下流胚的行径，是恶棍才干得出来的。”

“你没有什么好感激的。”他平静地说，随即松开了握着的那只柔软的手。“能遇上一个真正配得上一点帮助的人，是莫大的荣幸。谁出了名地有钱，谁就是现成的靶子，”他略带冷峭地笑了笑，“号称‘值得救助的对象’多得很，一茬一茬地送到他眼前。真正的难处，是找出那些需要帮助、却又不肯开口求人的。”

他真诚地相信，他这番话所对的那个女人，正属于这罕见的一类。

他霍地站起身。“我看见阿克顿夫妇了，”他喊道，“我约的是三点钟，在赌场门前——他们来得稍稍早了些。”

===Original===
Suddenly he got up. “I see the Actons,” he exclaimed. “I told them three o’clock in front of the Casino﻿—they’re a little before their time.”
It was a wonderful drive to Tréport, and Ivy, rather to her own surprise, enjoyed it. Partly, perhaps, because Rushworth’s old friend, James Acton, “fell for” her at once, to the amusement of his good-humoured, clever, middle-aged wife. They stopped at the Trianon Hotel on their way back and had some early tea; but even so it was only five o’clock when they returned to Dieppe and dropped the Actons.
Dismissing the car, they began walking towards the harbour. At last﻿—at last they were alone.
In the Grande Rue Ivy stopped, instinctively, before a minute shop, a branch of a famous house of the same name at Cannes and Deauville.

===Chinese===
去特雷波尔（Tréport）的一路兜得痛快极了，艾薇自己都有些意外，竟玩得尽兴。也许一半是因为，拉什沃思的老朋友詹姆斯·阿克顿（James Acton）一下子就拜倒在她的石榴裙下，惹得他那位脾气温和、聪明伶俐的中年太太直乐。归途他们在特里亚农饭店（Trianon Hotel）歇脚，早早用了茶点；饶是如此，回到迪耶普、让阿克顿夫妇下车时，也才五点钟。

打发走汽车，两人便朝海港信步走去。终于——终于只剩他们俩了。

走在大街（Grande Rue）上，艾薇身不由己地在一爿小店前停住了脚——那是一家名号响亮的商行的小小分号，同样的名号在戛纳（Cannes）和多维尔（Deauville）都开着铺子。

橱窗里只摆着一件东西；在拉什沃思这样的男人眼里，那是个模样相当可笑的小玩意儿——一只女用化妆包，看上去像个小巧的珍珠母长枕，搭扣是一颗大祖母绿，四围镶着珍珠。

===Original===
The window contained but one object, to Rushworth’s masculine eyes a rather absurd-looking trifle, for it consisted of a lady’s vanity bag which looked like a tiny bolster of mother-of-pearl. The clasp consisted of a large emerald set with pearls.
“What a lovely little bag!” exclaimed Ivy ecstatically.
“D’you like it?” Rushworth was filled with a kind of tender amusement. What a baby she was, after all!
“Like it? I adore it!”

===Chinese===
“多可爱的小包啊！”艾薇心醉神迷地喊道。

“你喜欢它？”拉什沃思心里涌起一种温存的笑意。她到底还是个孩子啊！

“喜欢？我爱死它了！”

“那我就把它送给你——就当是提前的圣诞礼物！”

===Original===
“Then I’ll give it you﻿—for next Christmas!”
“You mustn’t! It must be fearfully expensive,” she cried.
But he had already gone into the shop. With something like awe, she watched him from the pavement shovelling out bundles of thousand franc notes on to the narrow counter behind which stood a white-haired woman.
How rich, how enormously rich Miles Rushworth must be!

===Chinese===
“可不能这样！这一定贵得吓人。”她叫道。

可他已经走进店里去了。她站在人行道上，近乎敬畏地望着他朝那窄窄的柜台上一捆一捆地掏出成沓的一千法郎大钞，柜台后站着一位白发妇人。

迈尔斯·拉什沃思该多有钱，多得多么吓人啊！

他回到她身边时，艾薇看见那只贵重的包已经装进了一只柔软的皮匣子，显然是专为保护它定制的。他把这件叫人心花怒放的礼物放进她急切伸来的手里，含笑说道：

===Original===
As he joined her, Ivy saw that the precious bag was now enclosed in a soft leather case which had evidently been made for its protection. He put his delightful gift into her eager hands, and said, smiling:
“The elegant old dame in there﻿—she looked like a marquise herself﻿—declares that the clasp of this bag was once a brooch belonging to the Princesse de Lamballe, Marie Antoinette’s friend.”
“How wonderful!”
He looked at her quizzically, “I said I hoped it wouldn’t bring you bad luck! She quite understood the allusion,” which was more than Ivy did.

===Chinese===
“里头那位风致嫣然的老夫人——她那气度自己就像位侯爵夫人——一口咬定，这包上的搭扣原是朗巴勒公主（Princesse de Lamballe）的一枚胸针，就是玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）的那位朋友的。”

“多神奇呀！”

他揶揄地看了她一眼。“我说了，但愿它不会给你带来厄运！她一听就懂了我的典故。”——这一点可比艾薇强，艾薇可没听懂。

“看样子，这包是照一位太太的定单做的，搭扣上那颗宝石就是她自己的。她突然遭了丧事、穿起孝来，东西既是做出来了，店里便答应替她试着脱手。就在今天下午，它本来要发往多维尔分店的。在这儿搁了一个礼拜，老夫人承认，连一个问价的人都没有过！”

---

===Original===
“It was made, it seems, to the order of a lady who supplied the jewel for the clasp. She’s suddenly gone into mourning, and as they had made it they consented to try and sell it for her. It was being sent on to their Deauville branch this very afternoon. It’s been here a week, and the old lady admitted that she hadn’t had a single inquiry for it!”
Ivy had now opened, the case and taken out the wonderful little bag, her eyes dancing with pleasure and gratitude. She told herself with satisfaction, that, given the right kind of frock, she could use it by day as well as by night.
There was a very practical, shrewd side to Mrs. Jervis Lexton. But it was a side of her nature which she was slow to reveal to her men friends.

===Chinese===
这时艾薇已经打开皮匣，取出那件奇妙的小包，眼里闪着欢欣喜感激的光。她心满意足地盘算着：只要配上一件相称的衣裙，这包她白天也用得，晚上也用得。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太身上，还有十分精明务实的一面。只是她性子里的这一面，轻易不肯让她的男性朋友们瞧见。

===Original===
As they went on board the yacht a telegram was handed to Rushworth. Carelessly he tore it open, read it through, and then handed it to his guest:

> Tremendous affair taking place here tomorrow midday. French President unveiling monument to fallen. We propose staying the night in excellent hotel. Shall be back by teatime.
> Charlotte Chattle.

Ivy looked up. There was joy in Rushworth’s face﻿—and more than joy, for the eager, half-shamed look Ivy had so often seen on a man’s face was there also. But all he said was:

===Chinese===
两人登上游艇时，一份电报递到了拉什沃思手里。他漫不经心地撕开，从头到尾看完，然后递给了他的客人：

> 此地明日正午将有盛举。法国总统为阵亡将士纪念碑揭幕。我们拟在一所上好的旅馆过夜。傍晚茶点时分可归。
> 夏洛特·查特尔

艾薇抬起头来。拉什沃思脸上有喜色——而且不止是喜色，因为那副急切而略带羞惭的神情——艾薇在男人脸上见得多了——此刻也浮现在那里。但他只说了句：

===Original===
“This means that we shall have a quiet little dinner alone together, you and I.”
“That will be very nice,” she answered quietly.
“I’ve a good deal more work to get through so shall we say half-past eight? We might have dinner in what I call my sea-study. I always dine there, when I’m alone on the yacht.”
Just as she was leaving him, she turned and said gently:

===Chinese===
“这就是说，你我两人可以单独安安静静地吃一顿小晚饭了。”

“那太好了。”她轻声答道。

“我还有好些工作要赶完，那咱们就定八点半吧？晚饭可以在我称之为‘海上书斋’的地方吃。船上只我一个人的时候，我总在那儿用晚餐。”

她正要离开他时，又回过头来，柔声说：

===Original===
“Don’t you think you ought to have a little rest after all the work you did this morning? Why not wait till tomorrow?”
There was such a sweet solicitude in the tone in which she uttered those words that Rushworth felt touched.
“Work’s the only thing that makes time go by quickly,” he answered, and then, in a low, ardent tone, he added, “When I’m not with you, I’d far rather be working than idling﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你上午忙了那么半天，不觉得该稍稍歇一歇吗？何不等到明天再做呢？”

她说这话的语气里透着那么甜的关切，拉什沃思不禁为之动容。

“工作才是叫时间过得快的东西。”他答道，随即又用低沉而热切的声调补了一句：“不同你在一起的时候，我宁可工作，也不愿闲着——”

===Original===
A sensation of intense, secret triumph swept over Ivy Lexton. She felt that the gateless barrier Miles Rushworth had thrown up between them was giving way at last. Tonight would surely come her opportunity of lifting their ambiguous relationship from the dull plane of friendship to the exciting plane of what she called love.
She turned away, and then, a moment later, she stayed her steps, and looked back to where he was still standing.﻿ ﻿…
Small wonder that during the three hours that followed that informal parting, Rushworth, while mechanically dictating business letters, was gazing inwardly at a lovely vision﻿—an exquisite flower-like face and beseeching, beckoning eyes.

===Chinese===
一阵炽烈的、秘不示人的得意之情传遍了艾薇·莱克斯顿全身。她感到，迈尔斯·拉什沃思横亘在两人之间那道无门可越的屏障，终于开始瓦解了。今夜，她的机会想必必定要来了——好把她同他这段暧昧的关系，从友谊那层沉闷的层面，擢升到她所谓的爱情那个令人心荡神驰的层面。

她转身走了；片刻之后，她又收住脚步，回头望了望他仍伫立的地方。……

无怪乎在那番寻常道别之后的三个钟头里，拉什沃思一面机械地口授着业务信函，一面在心里凝望一个可爱的幻影——一张娇艳如花的面庞，一双既似恳求、又似召唤的眼睛。

## IV / 四

===Original===
When Ivy came out of her stateroom at half-past eight, the great heat of the day had gone, and old Dieppe harbour was bathed in a mysterious, enchanting twilight. She had put on tonight a white chiffon frock which made her look childishly young, and, as she floated wraithlike down the deck towards him, Rushworth caught his breath.

He had been waiting for her﻿—he would have been ashamed to acknowledge to himself for how long, though he knew that she was never late. Jervis had no sense of time, but punctuality was one of Ivy’s virtues.

“I’m afraid you’ll think my sea-study rather austere!”

===Chinese===
八点半，艾薇从她的舱房里走出来时，白日的酷热已经消退，古老的迪耶普（Dieppe）港浸在一片神秘而醉人的暮色里。今晚她穿了一条白色雪纺绸连衣裙，衬得人格外天真年轻；当她像幽魂般轻盈地飘过甲板朝他而去时，拉什沃思不觉屏住了呼吸。

他一直在等她——等了多久，他是不肯向自己承认的，虽然他明知她从不迟到。杰维斯毫无时间观念，而守时正是艾薇的美德之一。

「只怕你会觉得我这间海上书房太素净了！」

===Original===
“Austere?”

His lovely friend hardly knew the meaning of this, to her unusual, word. Eagerly she walked through into what was the floating workshop of a very busy man, though something had been done this evening to disguise its real character. Two great bowls of variously coloured roses stood on the writing-table; and in the centre of the stateroom was a small table set for two. On an Italian plate in the centre of the table was heaped up some fine fruit.

“How delicious!” She clapped her hands. “Who would ever think we were on board a ship?”

===Chinese===
「素净？」

他这位可爱的朋友几乎不懂这个字眼的意思——在她听来，这是个少见的字。她兴冲冲地走进去，走进这位大忙人的水上工作间；虽然今晚特意布置了一番，掩去了它的本来面目。写字台上摆着两大钵色彩缤纷的玫瑰；舱房正中摆着一张两人的小餐桌，桌子中央的一只意大利瓷盘里堆着精美的水果。

「多好呀！」她拍起手来。「谁能想得到我们是在一艘船上呢？」

===Original===
“You are the first guest of mine who has ever come through this door.”

He longed to tell her, he wanted her to know, that she held a place apart from any other human being in his life.

“How about Miss Chattle?”

===Chinese===
「你是我有生以来头一位走进这扇门的客人。」

他渴望告诉她，他要让她知道：在他的一生中，她占有一个不同于任何旁人的位置。

「那查特尔小姐（Miss Chattle）呢？」

===Original===
“Charlotte least of all! Once she was free of this room, I should never be able to get her out.”

“Not Miss Dale even?”

The colour rushed into Rushworth’s sunburnt face, and Ivy noted it with a jealous pang, as he answered more gravely, “No, not even Bella. I made up my mind﻿—and I’m a man who once he has made up his mind, well, sticks to it﻿—that this stateroom should be my bolt-hole, as well as my study. It’s understood by everyone on board that when I’m in here, I won’t be disturbed.”

He pointed to a telephone instrument. “If the yacht catches fire, my skipper has permission to ring me up.”

The short dinner was served well and quickly by Rushworth’s own steward; but neither of the two felt in the mood for talking in the presence of even the most unobtrusive third. Each was longing, consciously longing, to be alone with the other.

===Chinese===
「夏洛特（Charlotte）就更别提了！她只要进过这间屋子，我就再也休想把她请出去。」

「连戴尔小姐（Miss Dale）也没有吗？」

血色涌上拉什沃思那张晒得黝黑的脸，艾薇看在眼里，心头感到一阵醋意似的刺痛。他答得郑重了些：「没有，连贝拉（Bella）也没有。我早就打定了主意——我这个人是，主意一旦打定，呃，就一成不变——这间舱房既是我的书房，也是我的藏身之处（bolt-hole）。船上人人心里都有数：我待在这里头的时候，谁也不许来打扰。」

他指了指电话机。「哪怕是游艇着了火，也得船长才获准给我打电话。」

简短的晚餐由拉什沃思自己的侍者侍候，菜上得又好又快；可是他们俩谁也没有心思讲话，即便旁边侍立的是再不惹眼的人，到底也是第三个在场的人。两人都在渴望着——心里明明白白地渴望着——能单独待在一起。

===Original===
During that half hour when he had been waiting for her, aching for her, Miles Rushworth had faced up to the fact that he was madly in love with Ivy Lexton, and that he would give everything he valued most in the world to have her for his own.

But his passion for another man’s wife﻿—so again and again he assured himself﻿—was of an exalted, noble, and spiritual quality. Never would he allow that passion to become earthly. He admitted, at long last, that, as regarded his own peace of mind, he had been unwise to see as much of Ivy as he had done in London. But he had dreamt, fool that he had been, of a friendship which should be prolonged even after his marriage to another woman. During those three weeks he had also thought often of the girl whom he meant to make his wife. Ivy should be his friend and Bella’s friend﻿—their dear, dear friend.

But since they had all come together on his yacht Rushworth had had a rude awakening. He knew now that Bella Dale was his dear, dear friend, but Ivy Lexton was the woman he loved.

===Chinese===
在等她的那半个钟头里、苦苦盼她的那半个钟头里，迈尔斯·拉什沃思已经直面了一个事实：他疯狂地爱着艾薇·莱克斯顿，为了得到她，他甘愿舍弃这世上他最珍视的一切。

可是他对这个有夫之妇的恋情——他一遍又一遍地这样向自己保证——是脱俗的、高贵的、纯乎精神的。他绝不容这份感情沦为尘俗之爱。他终于承认，单就自己心境的安宁而论，他在伦敦时那样频繁地与艾薇见面，实在有欠明智。可他曾经梦想——他那时多傻呀——一种在他另娶之后仍能延续下去的友谊。在那三个星期里，他也常常想起那个他打算娶来做妻子的姑娘。艾薇将做他的朋友、贝拉的朋友——他们亲爱的、亲爱的朋友。

可是自从大家一同上了他的游艇，拉什沃思便被狠狠地惊醒了美梦。他如今明白了：贝拉·戴尔（Bella Dale）是他亲爱的、亲爱的朋友，而艾薇·莱克斯顿，才是他爱的女人。

---

===Original===
At last dinner, which had seemed to them both intolerably long, was over.

“Would you like coffee served on deck?” asked Rushworth.

But before Ivy could answer, the steward intervened. “It’s just begun to rain, sir,” and as he said the word “rain,” there came a flash of lightning, followed by a peal of thunder.

===Chinese===
那顿在两人看来都长得难以忍受的晚餐，总算结束了。

「咖啡要不要端到甲板上去用？」拉什沃思问。

艾薇还没来得及答话，侍者先开了口：「先生，刚开始下起雨来了。」他说出「雨」这个字时，一道闪电划过，紧接着响起一声炸雷。

===Original===
Ivy’s host turned to her. “We shall have to stay here, unless you’d rather go into the saloon?”

“I’d rather stay here,” she said in a low voice.

“Shall I pull down the blind, sir?”

===Chinese===
艾薇的东道主朝她转过身去。「我们只好待在这儿了——除非你想到大客厅去？」

「我宁愿待在这儿。」她低声说。

「要我把百叶窗放下来吗，先生？」

===Original===
“You may as well.”

In a minute the man brought in coffee, and then they heard him running along the deck through the pelting rain.

Ivy’s hand lay on the table. She looked at it﻿—her fingers were twitching. She felt, with joy and triumph, the tenseness of the atmosphere between them, and, for the first time, something in her responded to Rushworth’s still voiceless passion.

===Chinese===
「放下来也好。」

不一会儿那人便端来了咖啡，随后他们听见他冒着倾盆大雨顺着甲板一路跑远的声音。

艾薇的一只手搁在桌上。她望着这只手——手指在微微抽动。她怀着欢喜与得意，觉出两人之间的空气何其紧张；而且，生平头一遭，她心里的某个东西应和了拉什沃思那尚未出声的激情。

===Original===
“I don’t want any coffee,” she murmured.

“Neither do I.”

They both rose. He looked across at her. “You’ll find that sofa over there comfortable, I think.”

===Chinese===
「我不想喝咖啡。」她轻声说。

「我也不想。」

两人都站了起来。他隔着桌子望向她。「我想，那边那张沙发你坐起来会舒服的。」

===Original===
He uttered the commonplace words in a strained, preoccupied tone. The summer storm outside seemed at one with him, shutting them off from the world.

Ivy walked across to the little couch, which was just large enough for two, and, after a perceptible moment of hesitation, he followed her.

For a moment he stood silently gazing down into her upturned face. Then he began moving forward a chair.

===Chinese===
这几句平淡无奇的话，他是用一种紧绷绷的、心不在焉的声调说出来的。外面夏日的暴风雨仿佛与他声气相通，把这一双人与整个世界隔绝了开来。

艾薇走到那张小长沙发跟前——那沙发刚好容得下两个人——迟疑了看得出来的一瞬之后，他跟了过去。

他默默站了片刻，俯视着那张仰起的脸。然后他动手把一把椅子往前挪。

===Original===
“Won’t you sit down here, by me?” she asked, looking at him with her dove-like eyes.

“Shall I? Is there room?”

“Plenty of room,” she said tremulously.

===Chinese===
「你不在这儿坐下吗，就在我旁边？」她问，一双鸽子似的眼睛望着他。

「我坐吗？坐得下吗？」

「坐得下，宽绰得很呢。」她声音发颤地说。

===Original===
As he sat down, there came another vivid flash of lightning, followed by a peal of thunder louder than the one before.

He turned quickly to her: “You’re not frightened, are you?”

“I am﻿—a little.”

===Chinese===
他刚坐下，又是一道明晃晃的闪电，接着一声炸雷，比先前那一声更响。

他急忙转向她：「你不害怕吧？」

「怕——有一点儿。」

===Original===
And then all at once she was in his arms, and he was murmuring low, passionate words of endearment and of reassurance between each long, trembling, clinging kiss.

How he loved her! And how wonderful to know that she, poor darling, loved him too. He felt as may feel a man who, after wandering for days in the desert, suddenly comes on an oasis and a cool stream.

But even now, when every barrier between them seemed miraculously broken down, Rushworth kept a certain measure of control over himself.

===Chinese===
接着，冷不防地，她已经在他怀里了；在每一个悠长的、颤抖的、缠绵不舍的吻之间，他低低呢喃着炽热的情话与安慰。

他多么爱她啊！而知道她——这可怜的心肝——也爱他，又是多么美妙的事。他此刻的感受，宛如一个人在沙漠里跋涉了多日，忽然遇到一片绿洲、一道清泉。

可是即便到了这时——两人之间的一切藩篱似乎都已奇迹般地轰然倒塌——拉什沃思对自己仍保持着几分克制。

===Original===
“I’m going to pull up the blind and put out the light,” he whispered at last.

A moment later they were in darkness, though now and again a vivid flash of lightning illuminated the harbour through sheets of blinding, torrential rain.

He strode back to the little couch, and sank down again by her; but he resisted the aching longing to take her once more into his arms. Instead he took her soft hand in his, while he muttered in a broken voice, “I’ve been a brute! You must forgive me.”

===Chinese===
「我要把百叶窗拉上去，把灯灭了。」他终于低声道。

片刻之后，两人便置身黑暗之中，只有一道道耀眼的闪电，不时穿过瓢泼大雨的茫茫雨幕，照亮港口。

他大步走回小沙发，在她身旁重新坐了下来；但要把她再度拥入怀中的渴望那么锥心，他硬是忍住了。他只是把她柔软的手握在自己手里，声音断断续续地喃喃说：「我一直是个畜生！你一定得原谅我。」

===Original===
She answered in a stifled voice, “There is nothing to forgive.”

“I’ve been to blame all through!”

Then, in a tone he strove to lighten, “I ought to have labelled you ‘dangerous’ from the first moment I saw you.”

===Chinese===
她闷声答道：「没有什么可原谅的。」

「从头到尾都是我的不是！」

随后，他竭力换上轻松些的声调：「打见到你的头一眼起，我就该给你贴上『危险』的标签。」

===Original===
She melted into tears, and remorsefully he whispered, “Have I hurt you by saying that?”

She shook her head; but she pulled her hand away.

“Listen, Ivy?”

===Chinese===
她百感交集，化作眼泪扑簌簌落了下来；他懊悔地低声问：「我说这话，是不是伤着你了？」

她摇了摇头；可她把手抽了回去。

「听我说，艾薇？」

===Original===
It was the first time Miles Rushworth had called her by her name, and for that, Jervis, poor fool, had thought him old-fashioned and over-formal.

“Yes,” she whispered submissively.

“We’ve got to talk this out﻿—you and I.”

===Chinese===
这是迈尔斯·拉什沃思第一次直呼她的名字；还为着这个，杰维斯——可怜的傻瓜——曾觉得他老派、过分拘礼呢。

「是。」她恭顺地低声应道。

「咱们得把这件事谈个明白——你和我。」

===Original===
“Yes,” she said again, wondering what he meant by those strange words, and longing, consciously, even exultantly, longing, for him to take her again in his strong arms.

“I’ll begin by telling you something I’ve never told to any living being.”

He uttered those words in so serious a tone that Ivy felt a thrill of fear, of doubt, go through her. Had he a woman in his life whom he would not, or could not, give up?

===Chinese===
「是。」她又说了一遍，一面纳罕他这些古怪的话究竟是什么意思，一面渴望着——清清楚楚地、甚至欢欣鼓舞地渴望着——他再用他那强壮的手臂把她搂进怀里。

「我先告诉你一件我从未对任何活人说过的事。」

他说这几句话时口气郑重已极，艾薇只觉得一阵恐惧、一阵疑虑穿过全身。难道他的生活里有一个他不愿、或者不能放弃的女人？

===Original===
“I was twenty when my father died, and before his death we had a long private talk. Quite at the end of our talk, he made me give him a solemn promise.”

Rushworth stopped a moment. He was remembering what had been the most moving passage up to now in his thirty-five years of life. It was as if he heard the very tones of his father’s firm, if feeble, voice.

“At the time my promise seemed easy to keep. Indeed, I was surprised he thought it necessary to exact it.”

===Chinese===
「我父亲去世那年，我二十岁；临终之前，我们父子俩作了一次长谈。谈到快结束时，他要我向他立下一个庄严的诺言。」

拉什沃思停了片刻。他在回想他三十五年人生迄今最动人心魄的一段经历。他仿佛又听见了父亲说话的声口——虚弱，然而坚定。

「当时，那个诺言似乎不难遵守。说实话，他竟觉得有必要逼我立约，我还暗暗诧异呢。」

===Original===
“What was your promise?” Ivy whispered, and she came a little, only a little, nearer to him.

“My promise was never to allow myself to fall in love with a married woman. Though it hasn’t always been as easy as I thought it would be, till now I have kept that promise. But now I’ve broken it, for I love you. Love you? Why I adore you, my darling﻿—”

Again he waited, and Ivy felt oppressed, bewildered. Many men had said that they adored her. But no man had made that delightful, exciting admission, without showing strong apparent emotion.

===Chinese===
「你许的是什么诺言？」艾薇悄声问，同时朝他凑近了些——只凑近了一点点。

「我的诺言是：永远不容许自己爱上一个有夫之妇。虽说事情并不总像我想象的那么容易，可直到今日，我一直守着这个诺言。如今我破了戒，因为我爱你。爱你？唉，我是倾慕你啊，我的宝贝——」

他又停住了，艾薇只觉得又压抑又慌乱。说过倾慕她的男人多得很。可是从没有哪个男人吐露这句令人愉悦、令人心荡神驰的表白时，不带一丝外露的强烈感情。

===Original===
Rushworth had uttered the words calmly, collectedly, and staring straight before him.

“And I can’t help myself﻿—that’s the rub,” he went on, in the same matter-of-fact voice. “Indeed, I’m afraid I’m going to go on loving you all my life,” he smiled a rueful smile in the soft darkness which encompassed them.

“But of course I knew, even then, when I was a cub of twenty, what my father really meant. There is a part of my promise to him I can keep; and what’s more﻿—by God, I intend to keep it!”

===Chinese===
拉什沃思方才说那几句话时，平平静静、镇定自若，眼睛直望着前方。

「可我管不住自己——症结就在这里。」他接着说，用的还是那种就事论事的声调。「说实在的，恐怕我这一辈子都要爱你下去了。」他在包裹着两人的温柔黑暗中，绽出一丝苦笑。

「不过，当然，就连那时——我还是个二十岁的毛头小子的时候——我也明白父亲真正的用意。我对他许下的诺言，有一部分我是守得住的；不但如此——上帝在上，我打算守住它！」

===Original===
She was moved, thrown off her usual calculating balance, by the strength of his sincerity, and also made afraid.

“What d’you mean?” she faltered.

“It’s true that I love you﻿—I didn’t know there could be such love in the world as that which I feel for you, Ivy. If it would do you any good for me to jump into that harbour out there and be drowned, I’d do it! But I’m going to keep my love for you sacred, and I’m going not only to save myself, but I’m going to save you, my darling, darling love.”

===Chinese===
他那份真诚的力量把她打动了，搅乱了她惯常那副工于算计的平衡，同时也叫她害怕。

「你这话是什么意思？」她讷讷地说。

「我爱你是真的——艾薇，我从不知道世上能有我对你这样的爱。倘若我跳进外头那座港里淹死，能于你有半点好处，我立刻就去跳！可我要让我对你的爱保持圣洁；我不但要救我自己，还要救你，我的宝贝、宝贝的爱人。」

===Original===
He took her hand again, and this time he kissed it.

Ivy burst into bitter tears, and Rushworth put his arm around her.

“I know how you’re feeling,” he whispered brokenly. “My poor little darling! But for God’s sake don’t cry. I can’t bear it. You’ve nothing to be ashamed of﻿—it’s been all my fault.”

===Chinese===
他又拿起她的手，这一回，他吻了吻它。

艾薇伤心得泪如泉涌，拉什沃思伸出臂膀搂住了她。

「我知道你这会儿的心情。」他断断续续地低语。「我可怜的小宝贝！可是看在上帝分上，别哭了。我受不了。你没有什么可羞愧的——从头到尾都是我的错。」

===Original===
“Can’t we go on being friends? It’s been so wonderful having you for a friend!” she sobbed.

“Of course we’ll go on being friends﻿—dear, dear friends. But lovers﻿—no! I’m going right away﻿—it’s the only thing to do.”

He was telling himself that of course she did not understand﻿—how could she, gentle and pure if yet passionate creature that she was?﻿—the strength of his temptation. She would never know, indeed, he must never allow her to know, what she meant to him, and all he was about to give up for her sake. A sweet, loving wife, children, in a word, a happy, normal life﻿—all that Bella Dale had stood for in the secret places of his heart.

===Chinese===
「咱们不能继续做朋友吗？有你做朋友，一直是多么美好的事啊！」她抽抽噎噎地说。

「咱们当然是朋友——亲爱的、亲爱的朋友。可是做情人——不成！我就要远走了——只有这一条路可走。」

他在心里对自己说，她当然不明白——她又怎么可能明白呢，像她这样温柔、纯洁、却也不乏热情的人儿？——他所受的诱惑有多强劲。她永远不会知道——的确，他也万万不能让她知道——她于他意味着什么，以及他为了她的缘故即将舍弃的一切。一位温柔恩爱的妻子，儿女，一句话，一种幸福而正常的生活——这一切，正是贝拉·戴尔在他心底最隐秘的角落里所代表的东西。

===Original===
He was brought back to the present by her agitated, agonised, “Going away? Surely you’re not going away, now?”

He waited a moment without answering her. A frightful struggle was going on in his heart, his conscience. Then, at last, he answered the, to him, piteous question.

“Do you remember my once telling you of my sister? Of how I longed for you to know her﻿—but that she was too ill for me to take you to her.”

===Chinese===
她那激动而凄惶的一声「要走？你总不会现在就走吧？」把他唤回到了眼前。

他迟了片刻，没有回答她。一场可怕的斗争正在他的心里、他的良心里进行着。终于，他回答了这个在他听来凄楚可怜的问题。

「你可记得我有一回跟你提起过我妹妹？提起我多么盼望你能见见她——只是她病得太重，我不好带你去。」

===Original===
“Yes,” she murmured, trying to remember.

She had not been really interested, only secretly glad that Rushworth’s widowed sister was not well enough to see her. Ivy Lexton did not care to be brought in contact with her men friends’ mothers or sisters. They never liked her, and she never liked them.

“My sister saw a new specialist this week, and he says she ought to winter in South Africa. She’s horribly lonely﻿—her husband was killed in the war, and﻿—and now I’ve made up my mind to go with her. You do agree that it’s the best thing﻿—indeed, the only thing for me to do?”

===Chinese===
「记得。」她喃喃地说，努力回想着。

她当时并不怎么感兴趣，只是暗自庆幸拉什沃思那位寡居的妹妹身体不适，见不了她。艾薇·莱克斯顿可不喜欢同男性朋友的母亲或姊妹有什么来往。她们从来不喜欢她，她也从来不喜欢她们。

「我妹妹这星期见了一位新来的专科医生，医生说她应当去南非过冬。她孤单得可怜——她丈夫阵亡在了战场上，于是——于是我打定了主意陪她同去。你总该同意，这是我最好的一步——实在说，也是我唯一可走的一步吧？」

===Original===
There was something in his tone as he uttered the question that made her feel that, for the moment, at any rate, no plea would move him.

“I hate your going so far away,” she moaned.

“It’s the only thing to do,” he repeated in a hard tone.

===Chinese===
他发出这一问时，声调里有种东西使她感到，至少眼下一刻，无论怎么恳求都打动不了他。

「我恨你走得这么远。」她哀声说。

「只有这一条路可走。」他语气生硬地重说了一遍。

===Original===
“I’m afraid you despise me,” she said very low.

“Despise you? Good God! I honour you﻿—”

And then all at once she was again in his arms.

===Chinese===
「恐怕你是瞧不起我。」她声音低低地说。

「瞧不起你？老天爷！我是敬重你——」

接着，冷不防地，她又一次投进了他的怀抱。

===Original===
Moved out of her false selfish self by the strength and reality of his emotion, “I love you,” she murmured, clinging to him between their kisses. “I shall always love you,” and believed she spoke the truth.

Surely, surely, he wouldn’t go away now?

The door opened, and in the darkness they sprang apart.

===Chinese===
他这份感情的真挚与力量，把她从她那虚伪自私的自我里拔了出来；她偎依着他，在吻与吻的间隙里喃喃地说：「我爱你。我会永远爱你。」并且相信这话字字出自真心。

他总不会、总不会现在就走了吧？

门开了，两人在黑暗中霍地分开。

===Original===
“The hotel has sent the car for you, sir. It is now on the quay.”

“The car?”

A feeling of surprise and despondency swept over Ivy.

===Chinese===
「旅馆派车来接您了，先生。车这会儿已经停在码头上。」

「车？」

一阵惊愕与沮丧掠过艾薇的心头。

===Original===
Rushworth got up. For a moment or two, it seemed like eternity to him, he found he could not speak.

Then he said, “I’m afraid I must go now, Mrs. Lexton. I’m sleeping at the Hotel Royal tonight. A business friend of mine is staying there, and we are going to have a talk before turning in. He is going to Paris tomorrow morning.”

Addressing his servant: “I’ll be coming in a minute. The storm’s over, isn’t it?” he added.

===Chinese===
拉什沃思站起身来。有一两秒钟——于他恍如永恒——他发现自己开不了口。

然后他说：「恐怕我现在必须走了，莱克斯顿太太。今晚我睡在皇家饭店（Hotel Royal）。我有位生意上的朋友下榻在那儿，我们打算就寝前谈一谈。他明天早上动身去巴黎。」

他又对仆人说：「我这就出来。暴风雨已经过去了，是吧？」

===Original===
“I think it is, sir.”

“Then put on the light again, and take the despatch-box that’s over there on my writing-table to the car.”

Rushworth waited till the sounds of footsteps on the deck outside had grown faint. Then he came back to Ivy, but he had once more regained possession of himself.

===Chinese===
「我想是过去了，先生。」

「那么把灯重新点亮，把我写字台上那只公文箱搬到车上去。」

拉什沃思一直等到外面甲板上的脚步声渐渐微弱下去。然后他回到艾薇身边，不过他已经重新控制住了自己。

===Original===
“I want to tell you, now, what I didn’t mean to tell you till the last day of our trip. Some cousins of mine have a charming flat in the Duke of Kent Mansion, close to Kensington Gardens. They want to let it for six months, and I’ve just taken it in the hope that you and your husband will live there till you have found something you like better.”

“You’re too good to me.”

She looked crushed, defeated, humiliated.

===Chinese===
「我想现在就告诉你一件事，本来我打算到这趟旅行最后一天再说的。我有几位表亲在肯特公爵大厦（Duke of Kent Mansion）有一套很可爱的公寓，就在肯辛顿花园旁边。他们想把公寓租出去六个月，我刚把它租了下来，指望你和你的丈夫住进去，直到你们找到更合心意的房子为止。」

「你对我太好了。」

她看上去心力交瘁，一败涂地，羞惭无地。

===Original===
“Ivy! My precious darling﻿—” the yearning cry escaped him.

Slowly she lifted her head, and her eyes, swimming in tears, her trembling mouth, longing for his kisses, beckoned.

He leapt forward, and she fell upon his breast. “Must you go away? I don’t know how I shall live without you,” she sobbed.

As at last he tore himself from her arms, “Oh God,” he exclaimed. “If only you were free!”

===Chinese===
「艾薇！我珍贵的宝贝——」这声渴念的呼喊不由自主地挣脱而出。

她缓缓抬起头；那双噙满泪水的眼睛，那渴望着他亲吻的颤抖的嘴唇，都在向他召唤。

他一个箭步抢上前去，她扑倒在他胸前。「你一定得走吗？没有你，我不知道该怎么活。」她呜咽着说。

当他终于从她怀抱里挣脱出来时，「哦，上帝！」他喊道。「你若是个自由身该有多好！」

## IX / 九

===Original===

Roger Gretorex had gone back to his consulting-room after a long morning’s round. He loved his work, yet today his heart was not in it, for he was extraordinarily excited, and moved as he had never before felt moved in his twenty-eight years of life.

He had come up early from Sussex by a workman’s train, and had found waiting for him an undated and unsigned note in Ivy Lexton’s handwriting:

> I’m not quite sure if you are in London; but I know you will be very sorry to hear that Jervis died suddenly last night.
> I hardly know what I am doing﻿—the shock has been so great. Nurse says his heart must have given way. I would rather not see anybody for a little while.

===Chinese===

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯做完了漫长的上午巡诊，回到自己的诊室。他热爱本行，可今天心却不在上面，因为他格外亢奋，心头涌动着一种他二十八年的生命里从未体味过的激动。

他一早从萨塞克斯搭工人列车上来，发现有张没有日期、也不具名的便条在等着他，是艾薇·莱克斯顿的笔迹：

> 我说不准你在不在伦敦；但我知道，杰维斯昨晚突然去世的消息，你听了会非常难过。
> 我几乎不知道自己在做什么——打击太大了。护士说，他的心脏一定是撑不住了。眼下这一阵子，我谁都不想见。

===Original===

Now that his morning’s work was over, he was free to commune with his own thoughts, and to dwell on what the future now held for him﻿—a lifetime of bliss with the woman whom he worshipped, and who had given him the greatest proof of her love a woman can give a man.

His cherished darling was free﻿—free to become, after a decent interval had elapsed, his adored, honoured wife in the face of the world! He thanked God that he had never let his mother know the truth concerning their past relations. He thanked God again that the only time the two had met had been in those early days when he and Ivy had just been friends, and when he, at any rate, had thought that so they would remain.

True, his mother was far too clever, too devoted to him, her only child, not to guess, even then, that he was in love with Ivy. She had even ventured to say a word to him as to the danger of too close a friendship with a married woman. And he had bitterly resented it. He remembered her words, and his answer, “You’re wrong, mother. Ivy Lexton is the best and purest woman I have ever known!”

===Chinese===

上午的工作既已了结，他终于可以独自沉潜于自己的思绪，细想如今摆在他面前的未来——同他崇爱的那个女人共度一生的至福；而她，已经把一个女人所能给予男人的最重大的爱情证明给了他。

他视若珍宝的爱人自由了——只等挨过一段合乎体统的时日，便能当着世人的面，成为他敬爱的、名正言顺的妻子！他感谢上帝，从未让母亲知晓他们往日关系的真相。他再度感谢上帝：婆媳二人唯一的一次见面，还是在早年间——那时他和艾薇不过是朋友，至少他自己当时以为，两人会永远止乎此。

诚然，他母亲太聪明，也太疼他这个独子，即便在那时也不会猜不到他爱上了艾薇。她甚至壮着胆子提点过他一句，说同一个有夫之妇过从太密自有危险。而他当时怨愤得很。他记得她的话，也记得自己的回答：「您错了，母亲。艾薇·莱克斯顿是我平生所知最好、最纯洁的女人！」

===Original===

No wonder that, as he had gone in and out of the poor dwellings of his patients this morning, he had asked himself, again and again, how long it would be before he and Ivy could declare their love?

He remembered a war widow in their neighbourhood who had married again within four months of her husband’s being killed. Still, the world is very different in peacetime from what it is in wartime. All he would have to consider would be his own mother’s sense of what was right and fitting.

Ivy’s friends? His sensitive lips curled in disdain. They would scarcely be surprised if she remarried a week after her husband’s death!

Then, suddenly, there came over him a feeling which, to such a man as Roger Gretorex, was painfully like shame.

===Chinese===

怪不得今天上午，他在病人的贫寒住所里进进出出时，一遍又一遍地问自己：还要多久，他和艾薇才能公开他们的爱情？

他记得他们街区有位战争寡妇，丈夫阵亡不出四个月便改嫁了。话虽如此，和平年月的世界毕竟不同于战时。他所要顾虑的，唯有自己母亲心里那把对错与体统的尺子。

艾薇的那些朋友？他敏感的双唇鄙夷地一撇。丈夫死后一个星期她就再嫁，他们也不见得会吃惊！

接着，一种情绪突然攫住了他；对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯这样的人来说，那情绪痛切得近乎羞愧。

===Original===

Jervis Lexton had been something of a wastrel and all of a fool, but the young man had also been, according to his lights, a good husband. It was not Jervis’s fault that Ivy had never loved him. Her heartless, money-loving mother had forced her into the marriage when she was almost a child. Such was the story she had told Gretorex, and that story he implicitly believed.

He told himself that the only decent thing to do, now, would be to write her a short note of regret and sympathy, as cold and colourless as hers had been.

How he longed, how he ached, to see her! But it was clear she wished to see no one, not even him, the one closest to her, yet.

The long morning of pent-up emotion, and of really hard work, had tired him out﻿—made him feel, too, suddenly very hungry. He got up and took his hat off the peg on the door, intending to snatch a hasty meal at a restaurant in Victoria Street hard by.

===Chinese===

杰维斯·莱克斯顿固然多少是个浪荡子，而且彻头彻尾是个蠢人，可就这个青年自己的处世之道而言，他也算得上一位好丈夫。艾薇从未爱过他，那不是杰维斯的过错。是她那无情而贪财的母亲，在她几乎还是个孩子的时候，硬把她塞进了这门婚事。这是她对格雷托雷克斯讲的说法，而他对这说法深信不疑。

他告诉自己，眼下唯一得体的做法，是给她写一封简短的吊唁慰问信，写得同她那封一样冷淡、一样平板。

他多么渴望见到她，想得心口发疼！可她显然谁也不愿见——就连他这个与她最亲的人，眼下也不例外。

一上午积压的情绪，加上实打实的操劳，早把他拖得疲惫不堪——还让他突然觉得饿得很了。他站起身，从门上的挂钉摘下帽子，打算就近去维多利亚街（Victoria Street）一家餐馆匆匆吃一顿。

===Original===

Then, just as he was turning towards the door, the telephone bell rang. With a feeling of irritation he took up the receiver.

“Yes?” he called out impatiently.

And then there came over him a thrill of intense joy, for the voice which said in a tremulous tone, “Is that you, darling?” was Ivy Lexton’s voice.

She had not called him “darling” once, since her return to London, and that though he knew she often used the endearing term, even to the pet dogs of her women friends.

===Chinese===

就在他转身朝门口走去时，电话铃响了。他心头一阵烦躁，拿起了听筒。

「喂？」他不耐烦地应了一声。

随即，一阵强烈的喜悦贯穿全身——那个用颤抖的声调问「是你吗，亲爱的？」的声音，正是艾薇·莱克斯顿的声音。

自打她回到伦敦，一次也没叫过他「亲爱的」——尽管他知道，这爱称她常挂在嘴边，连女友们的爱犬她也这么叫。

===Original===

“Of course it is,” he answered tenderly. “How are you, dearest? A little less tired and”﻿—he forced himself to add the word﻿—“unhappy?”

And then he heard her voice again; but now it was full of a kind of cold urgency.

“I’ve something so dreadfully important to say to you﻿—are you alone in the house?”

“Absolutely alone,” he called back reassuringly.

He did not count Mrs. Huntley, the old woman who lived a door or two off, and who “did” for him, as anybody.

===Chinese===

「当然是我。」他柔声答道，「你好吗，最亲爱的？不那么累了，也不那么——」他硬逼着自己补出那个词——「难过了？」

接着他又听见了她的声音；可这一次，那声音里满是某种冷冰冰的急迫。

「我有件要紧得不得了的事要对你说——屋里就你一个人吗？」

「就我一个人。」他宽慰地回了一声。

他没有把亨特利太太算在内——那老太太住在隔一两个门的地方，替他「打杂」料理家务，在他眼里算不得什么人。

===Original===

“Please don’t say my name, and I won’t say yours. Telephones are tapped sometimes, and I’m so﻿—so frightened,” came the whispered words.

There followed a long pause, and Gretorex suddenly felt filled with an unreasoning sensation of acute apprehension. There had been that in Ivy’s tremulous tones which he had never heard there before﻿—a note of horrible fear.

“Are you listening?” came at last the beloved voice, sounding now startlingly near.

“I can hear you perfectly.”

“Something so dreadful has happened! I don’t know how to tell you. It’s so﻿—so strange. You’d never guess what it was!”

===Chinese===

「请别说出我的名字，我也不说你的。电话有时候会叫人窃听，我好怕——好害怕。」传来的是压得低低的话语。

接着是长长的静默，格雷托雷克斯骤然被一种莫可名状的尖锐忧惧攫住。艾薇那颤抖的声调里，有一样他从未听到过的东西——一丝骇人的惧意。

「你在听吗？」那令他爱恋的声音终于又响起，此刻近得吓人。

「我听得清清楚楚。」

「出了一件可怕透顶的事！我不知道怎么说出口。这事太——太蹊跷了。你怎么也猜不到是什么！」

===Original===

He tried to curb his anxiety, his suspense, his impatience.

“What is it that has happened?” he asked quietly.

Again there followed a long unnatural pause. Then, at last, Ivy Lexton breathed the words:

“The doctors found out yesterday that poor﻿—you know who I mean﻿—did not die what they call a natural death.”

“Not a natural death?” he repeated in a tone of amazement. “What do you mean, darling?”

“They say he died of some kind of poison.”

===Chinese===

他竭力按捺住自己的焦灼、悬念和急躁。

「到底出了什么事？」他平静地问。

又是一阵长长的、反常的沉默。末了，艾薇·莱克斯顿才吐出那几句话：

「医生们昨天查出来了，可怜的——你知道我说的是谁——死得并不是他们所谓的那种自然死亡。」

「不是自然死亡？」他愕然重复道，「你这是什么意思，亲爱的？」

「他们说他死于某种毒药。」

===Original===

“Poison! D’you mean he committed suicide?” he asked incredulously.

“Oh, no, they don’t think that.”

Then, in a tone of great relief, she added: “But I suppose he may have done so.”

Gretorex felt not only exceedingly surprised, but inexpressibly shocked as well.

“I should be very loth to believe that,” he said at last.

===Chinese===

「毒药！你是说他自寻短见了？」他难以置信地问。

「哦，不，他们不这么认为。」

随后她用大大松了口气似的声调补了一句：「不过我想，他倒也可能是自己寻了短见。」

格雷托雷克斯不仅万分诧异，更感到难以言表的震惊。

「我很不愿意相信这种事。」他终于说道。

===Original===

“What I really want to tell you is that a dreadful man has been to see me this morning. He’s only been gone about half an hour. I was afraid to telephone from my own﻿—” She waited a moment, then uttered the word “house.” “I’m speaking from a call office.”

“What did the man say? Who was he?” he asked.

“He had to do with the police and he said he was going to see you as soon as he’d had something to eat. I said you generally went to your club to lunch, and that you wouldn’t be back before three.”

“Why should he want to see me?” Gretorex said wonderingly.

“He seemed to know so much about you. So much”﻿—her voice sank﻿—“about *us*. He asked me such funny questions, darling. Of course I told him﻿—I told him,” her voice faltered, “that you were just a great friend of mine and of﻿—you know of whom?”

===Chinese===

「我真正要告诉你的是，今天上午有个可怕的男人来见过我。他走了才半个钟头左右。我不敢从自己的——」她顿了顿，才吐出「家里」二字。「我这是在公用电话亭（call office）打的电话。」

「那人说了什么？他是什么人？」他问。

「他是同警方打交道的人，他说一吃过东西就要来见你。我说你午饭通常都去俱乐部吃，三点之前回不来。」

「他为什么要见我？」格雷托雷克斯纳闷地说。

「他好像知道你好多事。好多——」她的声音沉了下去，「关于*我们俩*的事。他问了我好些古怪的问题，亲爱的。我当然告诉他——我告诉他，」她的声音发起颤来，「说你只不过是我的一位要好朋友，还有——你知道是谁的朋友吧？」

===Original===

“So I am. So I was﻿—”

But Roger Gretorex was no fool, and his whole being had become flooded, these last few moments, with an awful sensation of dismay and foreboding.

“Tell me exactly what it was this man asked you, and what you said to him, my pet?”

He tried to make his voice sound confident and reassuring.

“I can’t tell you everything over the telephone. It would take too long. He wasn’t really disagreeable. In fact, we ended up quite good friends. But he said it was his duty to find out the truth, as that horrible man﻿—you know whom I mean?”

===Chinese===

「我确实是。我向来是——」

可罗杰·格雷托雷克斯不是傻子；这最后几分钟里，一种可怕的惊惶与不祥的预感，早已漫过了他的整个身心。

「原原本本告诉我，那人问了些什么，你又怎么答的，我的小宝贝？」

他竭力让自己的嗓音听上去笃定，能宽慰人。

「电话里没法把一切都告诉你，那说来话就长了。其实他倒不怎么讨人嫌。到后来我们简直成了好朋友。可他说，查明真相是他的职责，因为那个可恶的男人——你知道我指的是谁吧？」

===Original===

“No,” he called back rather sharply, “I have no idea whom you mean! Can’t you speak plainly, darling? No one is in the least likely to be listening over the wire.”

And then she breathed the one name that she did breathe during that strange, to Gretorex that terrible and ominous, telephone conversation.

“I mean Dr. Berwick, of course. He told them, I suppose, about you.”

“Who do you mean by ‘them’?”

“The people at Scotland Yard.”

“But what could Dr. Berwick tell anybody about me?”

===Chinese===

「不知道，」他相当生硬地回道，「我根本不知道你指谁！你就不能说明白些吗，亲爱的？总不会有人在线上偷听。」

随后，她轻轻吐出了一个名字——在那场古怪的、在格雷托雷克斯听来可怖而不祥的电话里，她唯一吐出的一个名字。

「我当然是指伯威克医生。我想，是他对他们讲了你的事。」

「你说的『他们』是谁？」

「苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的人。」

「可伯威克医生能对别人讲出我什么来？」

===Original===

“That you used to come to the flat﻿—that we were friends.”

And then, in an imploring voice that was scarcely audible, she murmured:

“You won’t give me away, dear? You will never let anyone know that﻿—”

Interrupting her he exclaimed, “There’s nothing to give away! You and I have only been friends﻿—nothing more.”

He felt a thrill of relief when she said, in a more natural tone:

===Chinese===

「说你从前常到公寓来——说我们是朋友。」

随后，她用一种几乎听不见的恳求声低语道：

「你不会出卖我的，对吧，亲爱的？你永远不会让任何人知道那件——」

他打断她，扬声道：「没什么可出卖的！你我不过是朋友——仅此而已。」

待她用比较自然的口吻说出下面的话时，他感到一阵宽慰：

===Original===

“That’s exactly what I said. I mean that’s what I told the man who came from Scotland Yard. I think he did believe me at last, but﻿—”

“Yes?” asked Gretorex anxiously. “But what, my dear?”

“I was silly enough to let out that you had been rather fond of me, in a sort of a way.”

“I’m sorry you did that. I’m afraid that was a mistake. I mean﻿—”

“I know what you mean! The moment I’d said it I saw what a mistake I’d made! But he spoke as if he already knew such a lot, or at any rate, some part of it.”

===Chinese===

「我正是这么说的。我是说，我就是这么告诉那个从苏格兰场来的人的。我想他最后确实信了我的话，可是——」

「可是什么？」格雷托雷克斯焦急地问，「怎么了，亲爱的？」

「我傻乎乎地漏了口风，说你多少有些喜欢过我，有那么几分意思。」

「你这么说我很难过。恐怕这是个失着。我是说——」

「我知道你想说什么！话一出口我就明白自己铸成了大错！可他那口气，仿佛本来就知道好多，至少也知道一部分。」

===Original===

He said patiently, “What part of it?”

“That even if I didn’t care for you, you had been very fond of me.”

“I don’t see that our private affairs are anyone’s business but our own,” he said savagely.

She answered despairingly, “Neither do I. But there it is! I know he’ll talk about me to you.”

Gretorex felt as if he were living through a hideous nightmare. What could, what did, all that Ivy had said, and was saying, mean?

“There’s something else I must tell you and warn you about, before I ring off. The man actually asked me, darling, if I’d ever been to see you﻿—I mean alone. Of course I said no, that I had never been alone to see you. Why should I? But I did tell him about the time I came to tea with Rose Arundell, when Captain Chichester came too. The man from Scotland Yard is sure to ask you about that﻿—at least I’m afraid so.”

===Chinese===

他耐着性子问：「哪一部分？」

「就是说，纵然我无心于你，你却一直非常喜欢我。」

「我不明白，你我之间的私事，除了我们自己还与什么人相干。」他狠狠地说。

她绝望地答道：「我也不明白。可事情已经这样了！我知道他会在你面前议论我。」

格雷托雷克斯只觉得自己正陷在一场狰狞的梦魇里。艾薇方才说过的、此刻正在说的这一切，能是什么意思？究竟是什么意思？

「挂电话之前，我还有件事必须告诉你，提醒你。那人竟然问我，亲爱的，问我去没去见过你——我是说单独去。我当然说没有，说我从未单独去见过你。我为什么要去呢？不过我确实跟他提了那回我同罗丝·阿伦德尔一起去喝茶、奇切斯特上尉（Captain Chichester）也来了的事。苏格兰场那人准会问你这个——至少我怕是这样。」

===Original===

“About my tea-party? Why should he?”

“No, no,” she cried shrilly.

Then, in a low tone, she uttered the words, “He’ll certainly ask you whether I ever came to see you alone, at Ferry Place. Don’t you understand?”

“I hear what you say. But everyone we know is aware that we’ve been great friends. There’s no mystery about it.”

“That’s what I said. And also that you were so fond of﻿—you know who, and he so fond of you.”

===Chinese===

「问我那次茶会？他为什么要问？」

「不，不是，」她尖声叫道。

随后她压低嗓音说：「他肯定会问你，我到底有没有单独去费里街见过你。你还不明白吗？」

「你的话我听见了。可我们认识的人谁都知道，我们是要好的朋友。这里面毫无秘密可言。」

「我也是这么说的。我还说，你那么喜欢——你知道的那位，而他也那么喜欢你。」

===Original===

To that Gretorex made no answer. In a sense it was true that poor Jervis Lexton had become quite fond of him, and that this was so had made him feel wretched and ashamed.

“Forgive me for having worried you, dear﻿—”

There was something﻿—he would not even to himself use the words﻿—cringing, even abject, in the tone in which she uttered that poor little sentence.

He answered at once, “You could never worry me, my darling! I can’t help thinking there’s some queer, spiteful enemy of yours, some cruel woman, behind all this?”

She cried hysterically, “It’s a spiteful, cruel man! It’s Dr. Berwick﻿—I know it is!”

===Chinese===

这话格雷托雷克斯没有接茬。从某种意义上说，可怜的杰维斯·莱克斯顿确实变得相当喜欢他，而事实如此，也正让他感到痛苦而羞愧。

「原谅我让你烦心了，亲爱的——」

她说出这句可怜巴巴的话时，语气里有种东西——连对他自己，他都不肯用出那两个字——卑屈，甚至是低声下气。

他立刻答道：「你永远不会让我烦心，我亲爱的！我总不免觉得，这一切背后藏着你哪个古怪刁毒的冤家，哪个狠毒的女人？」

她歇斯底里地叫道：「是个歹毒、狠心的男人！是伯威克医生——我知道就是他！」

===Original===

“But why d’you think that, darling?”

Gretorex waited a moment, then asked in almost a whisper, “Was he fond of you? Did he make love to you?”

She was so long in answering his question that, for a moment, he thought they had been cut off. Then he heard the muffled reply, “Not exactly, though of course he liked me. But﻿—but he hated you! I do know that.”

“I see,” and he thought that he did.

“Dr. Berwick wouldn’t sign some kind of a certificate which nurse says a doctor always has to sign when a person dies,” she went on. “You know what I mean?”

===Chinese===

「可你为什么这么想呢，亲爱的？」

格雷托雷克斯等了片刻，然后近乎耳语地问：「他喜欢你吗？他向你求过爱吗？」

她迟迟不答，有一会儿工夫，他都以为线路断了。随后他听到那声闷闷的回答：「说不上，不过他当然喜欢过我。可是——可是他恨你！这个我确实知道。」

「我明白了。」而且他自认为的确明白了。

「伯威克医生不肯签一种什么证明书——护士说，人死了，医生总得签那么一张的，」她继续道，「你明白我的意思吧？」

===Original===

“Yes.”

“That’s why they had what is called a postmortem, and found out my poor sweet had been﻿—” her voice faltered.

It was, even now, like a blow between the eyes for Gretorex to hear Ivy call Jervis “my poor sweet.”

Again she waited a while then he heard her whispered, agitated, half-question:

“I do so wonder what that man will say to you? I feel so horribly nervous.”

===Chinese===

「明白。」

「所以才做了那种叫验尸的检查，查出我可怜的心肝是——」她的声音颤了颤。

直到此刻，听见艾薇管杰维斯叫「我可怜的心肝」，对格雷托雷克斯来说，仍像照着眉心挨了一记重击。

她又停了片刻，随后他听见她那句低低的、心神不宁的半是问话：

「我实在想知道那人会对你说些什么？我心里紧张得要命。」

===Original===

He said impatiently, “I don’t suppose he’ll say much. But of course it’s the business of the police to get in touch with everyone who can throw even a little light on a mysterious death.”

“You’ll be very, very careful?”

For the moment he could not think what she meant.

Then, with a painful feeling of self-rebuke and fear, he hastened to reassure her, “Of course I will! Not that there’s anything to be careful about.”

“I must go home now,” and he heard her blow him a kiss.

===Chinese===

他不耐烦地说：「我想他说不了什么。不过，但凡能为一桩离奇的死亡照亮一星半点的人，警方照例都要一一寻访。」

「你会千万、千万小心的吧？」

一时之间，他竟想不明白她这话的意思。

随即，带着一种痛切的自责与忧惧，他赶紧宽慰她：「我当然会小心！倒不是说真有什么要小心的。」

「我现在得回家了。」说着，他听见她给他飞了个吻。

===Original===

She hadn’t done that for﻿—it seemed an eternity to him.

He hung up the receiver, went across to his writing-table, and sat down. He must think hard, and prepare some sort of story. But even now he could not imagine why his name, his personality, were being brought into this mysterious affair of Jervis Lexton’s sudden death.

Jervis Lexton’s death caused by poison? And the police already making inquiries? The whole story sounded incredible to Roger Gretorex. He told himself that of course some extraordinary mistake had been made. But whose mistake?

His mind turned at last to Dr. Berwick. He had only seen the man once﻿—and a damned offensive fellow he had seemed to be! So much did Gretorex remember. But Berwick was more than that﻿—he was a blackguard who had made love to a patient’s wife.

Poor little Ivy! Poor precious little love! No wonder she had been frightened, made quite unlike her gay, brave self, by the ordeal she had just gone through. How he longed to go and seize her in his arms, to bear her away to some place where they could be just themselves﻿—lovers!

===Chinese===

她有多久没这样了——在他看来，仿佛已隔了一世。

他挂上听筒，走到写字台前坐下。他必须好好想一想，编出一套说辞来。可直到此刻，他仍想不明白：自己的名字、自己这个人，怎么会牵进杰维斯·莱克斯顿猝死这桩离奇的案子。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿死于毒药？警方已经在查问了？这整套说法在罗杰·格雷托雷克斯听来匪夷所思。他对自己说，这必定是出了什么非同小可的差错。可又是谁的差错呢？

他的思绪终于转到伯威克医生身上。此人他只见过一面——而且当时就显得是个混账透顶的家伙！这一点格雷托雷克斯记得清楚。可伯威克不止如此——他是个向病人之妻求爱的无赖。

可怜的小艾薇！可怜的、娇贵的小爱人！难怪她受了惊吓，全然不像平日那个快活、勇敢的自己——她刚熬过了那样一场磨难。他多么渴望奔过去把她搂进怀里，带她远走一方天地，在那里他们可以只做他们自己——一对恋人！

===Original===

The thought of a crowded restaurant was intolerable. He no longer felt hungry. Besides, the man, he supposed him to be a detective, mentioned by Ivy, would soon be here.

All at once he heard the sounds made by a broom in the passage outside.

He opened the door. “Will you come in here for a moment, Mrs. Huntley?”

The old woman shuffled into the room, and he looked at her fixedly.

“I feel very tired today﻿—too tired to go out.”

Taking a two-shilling piece out of his pocket, he handed it to her: “Will you get me some pressed beef or ham? I suppose there’s bread and butter in the house? I’m ashamed to bother you, for I know you’re in a hurry to get home.”

===Chinese===

一想到人声鼎沸的餐馆，他就无法忍受。他已不觉得饿了。况且，艾薇提到的那个人——他料想是位探员——很快就要到了。

蓦地，他听见外面过道里传来扫帚的声响。

他打开房门。「能进来待一会儿吗，亨特利太太？」

老太太拖着脚步进了屋，他定定地望着她。

「我今天累得很——累得不想出门了。」

他从口袋里摸出一枚两先令的硬币递给她：「能给我买点儿压制牛肉（pressed beef）或者火腿来吗？家里想必还有黄油面包吧？真不好意思麻烦您，我知道您急着回家。」

===Original===

Said Mrs. Huntley, with a rather pathetic laugh, “I’d do a good bit more than that for you, doctor! Why, I’d go to any trouble for you.”

“Mrs. Huntley?”

He moved a little nearer to the old woman.

“You’ve just said that you’d go to any trouble for me﻿—”

“Ay, and so I would! I’ll never forget how good you were to that poor daughter of mine. Why, it’s thanks to you that she died easy. I’m not likely to forget that, however long I may live.”

===Chinese===

亨特利太太带着几分凄然的笑说：「为您，比这多得多的事我都肯做，大夫！嗐，为您什么麻烦我都不怕。」

「亨特利太太？」

他朝老太太挪近了些。

「您刚才说，为了我您什么麻烦都不怕——」

「是啊，我说到做到！我永远忘不了您对我那苦命的闺女有多好。嗐，多亏了您，她才走得安稳。这话我活多久就记多久，一辈子忘不了。」

===Original===

“The time has come when you can do something﻿—something very important﻿—for me,” he said, wondering if he were being wise or foolish.

“Can I, sir? You’ve only got to say what it is. I don’t mind no trouble.”

“I regard you,” he said slowly, “as a very superior person, as well as a very trustworthy one, Mrs. Huntley.”

She grew red with pleasure at his kind, flattering words, and, troubled as he was, Gretorex’s heart went out to her.

“All I want you to do,” he went on, “is to hold your tongue on my behalf. The time may come when you will be asked what sort of visitors I have received since I came to live here. You may be questioned as to whether any ladies ever came to see me﻿—”

He waited a moment, feeling acutely uncomfortable at having to ask the old woman to lie for him.

===Chinese===

「时候到了，您可以为我做一件事——一件顶要紧的事了。」他说着，心里却拿不准这一步是智是愚。

「我能吗，先生？您只管吩咐是什么事。什么麻烦我都不在乎。」

「我把您，」他缓缓地说，「看作一个不同凡俗的人，也是一个极其可靠的人，亨特利太太。」

听了这番善意奉承的话，她高兴得涨红了脸；格雷托雷克斯尽管满腹心事，一颗心也不由得向她倾了过去。

「我要您做的只有一件事，」他接着说，「就是替我把嘴闭严。往后说不定会有人问，我搬来这儿住以后都接待过些什么样的访客。人家兴许还会问，有没有哪位太太小姐来看过我——」

他顿了顿，为不得不求这老太太替自己撒谎而感到万分难堪。

===Original===

“You will be doing me a great service, Mrs. Huntley, if you will answer that no friends ever come to see me unless they have an appointment. Also that, to the best of your belief, the only time you have ever seen any lady here was when I gave a tea-party some time ago. Do I make myself quite clear?”

“Yes, sir, quite clear.”

“And have I your promise?”

“Yes, sir, you have my promise.”

He took her withered, work-worn hand in his.

===Chinese===

「要是人家问起，您就这么答：从不曾有朋友不经预约就来看我；还有，就您所知，您唯一一次在这儿见到哪位太太小姐，是前些时候我办茶会的那一回。您若这样回答，就是帮了我一个大忙，亨特利太太。我说得够清楚了吗？」

「清楚了，先生，够清楚的了。」

「您答应我了？」

「答应了，先生，我向您保证。」

他把她那只枯瘦的、劳作磨损的手握进了自己手里。

===Original===

“I’m very grateful to you. This may mean more to me than you will ever know.”

“I’ll go and get the things for your lunch, sir.”

She shut the door behind her, and a moment later, as he saw her pass the window, a hot tide of humiliation seemed to overwhelm him. He had seen, by the expression on her face, that everything there was to know, she knew.

As for Mrs. Huntley, she felt quite sure that Dr. Gretorex, who, though she knew him to be far from well off, had spared neither time nor money in his care of her dying daughter, was about to figure as a corespondent in a divorce case.

Well, in so far as she could help him, she’d do anything. Lie for him? Of course she would! Where’s the good of caring for a person if you’re not willing to do anything for him or her? Such was Mrs. Huntley’s simple philosophy of life. She was a good hater as well as a good lover. In her fashion she loved Gretorex, but she hated Ivy Lexton.

Those who are called “the poor” are seldom deceived in a man’s or a woman’s real nature and character. They are too close up against the hard realities of life to make many mistakes. It requires no touchstone to teach them the difference between dross and gold.

===Chinese===

「我非常感激您。这件事对我的干系之重，也许远不是您所能知道的。」

「我这就去给您买午饭的东西，先生。」

她随手带上了门。片刻之后，望着她的身影从窗前经过，一股滚烫的屈辱潮水劈头盖顶地涌来，几乎将他吞没。他从她脸上的神情看出来：凡是可知的一切，她全知道了。

至于亨特利太太，她认定格雷托雷克斯医生就要在一桩离婚案里以「共同被告」（corespondent）的身份出场了——她明知他手头远说不上宽裕，可在她那垂死的闺女身上，他费的心血和钱财从来不曾吝惜过。

嗐，只要帮得上他，她什么都肯做。为他撒谎？那还用说，她当然肯！疼一个人，若不肯为他出力，那份疼又算得什么疼？这便是亨特利太太朴素的人生哲学。她爱得真切，恨得也真切。她以她自己的方式爱着格雷托雷克斯，却恨透了艾薇·莱克斯顿。

那些被称作「穷人」的人，在判断一个男人或女人的真实本性与人品时，很少看走眼。他们贴着生活的硬实底子过活，出不了多少岔子。渣滓与真金有什么分别，无需试金石来教他们。

---

===Original===

About three o’clock the telephone bell rang again.

Gretorex hoped for a moment to hear Ivy’s voice again, but it was a man who asked, “Can I speak to Dr. Roger Gretorex?”

“My name is Roger Gretorex.”

“I have a matter of business to discuss with you, Dr. Gretorex; and I’m telephoning to know if I may come along now, as you are in?”

===Chinese===

三点钟左右，电话铃又响了。

格雷托雷克斯一时间指望再听到艾薇的声音，可发问的却是个男人：「请问我能同罗杰·格雷托雷克斯医生通话吗？」

「我就是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯。」

「我有件公事要同您商谈，格雷托雷克斯医生；您既然在家，我打电话来问问，眼下是否可以登门。」

===Original===

“Pray do so. But may I ask your name? And would you mind telling me your business?” he called back.

“My name is Orpington. As to my business, it would take too long to explain. But I will be with you in a very few minutes.”

Mechanically Gretorex began to tidy his consulting-room. For the first time in his life he felt horribly afraid, he knew not of what, but that made his dread of the coming inquisition all the sharper, the fuller of suspense.

His mother had managed to keep one conservatory going, and though he had given away a good many of the flowers he had brought up with him this morning, there was still a lovely nosegay on his writing-table. And the sight of these fragrant blossoms recalled poignantly to her son’s mind the woman who had gathered them for him. Was he going to bring sorrow, and what to her would be worse than sorrow﻿—shame﻿—on her honoured name?

===Chinese===

「请务必过来。不过可否请教尊姓大名？还有，可否见告您的来意？」他回道。

「我叫奥平顿（Orpington）。至于来意，说起来话长。不过几分钟后我就到您那儿了。」

格雷托雷克斯木然动手整理起诊室来。他有生以来第一次感到骇人的恐惧，却不知恐惧的是什么；而这一点，反倒让他对行将到来的盘问愈发胆寒，愈发悬心。

他母亲好歹还撑着一间温室；今早他随身带上来的花，虽说已经送出去不少，写字台上仍留着一束可爱的小花。这些芬芳花朵的影子，让做儿子的心头蓦地一阵锥刺，想起了那位为他采下它们的女人。他难道要给她的名字——那受人敬重的名字——带上悲哀，乃至对她而言比悲哀更糟的耻辱吗？

## Prologue / 序章
===Original===
“Tell me something about the Lextons, Mary. Where did you pick them up?” asked Lady Flora Desmond of her hostess, Mrs. Hampton. “As I looked at Mrs. Lexton during dinner, I thought I had never seen a prettier face. When I was a child, your little friend would have been what people then called a professional beauty.”
“She certainly is very pretty, and a regular honeypot! Look at her now, with Miles Rushworth?”
The speaker nodded towards the wide-open French window of the high-ceilinged, oval eighteenth-century sitting-room. She and two other women were sitting there together after dinner, on the Saturday evening of what Mrs. Hampton thought promised to be a very successful weekend party.
The window gave access to a broad stone terrace. Beyond the terrace lay a wide lawn, bathed in bright moonlight, and across the lawn sauntered very slowly two figures, that of a tall man, and that of a slender woman dressed in a light-coloured frock. They were moving away from the beautiful old country-house where they were both staying as guests, making for an avenue of beeches.

===Chinese===
“跟我讲讲莱克斯顿家的事吧，玛丽。你是在哪儿结识他们的？”弗洛拉·德斯蒙德小姐（Lady Flora Desmond）问她的女主人汉普顿太太。“方才席间我瞧着莱克斯顿太太，心想从没见过比这更好看的面孔。在我小时候，你这位小朋友准会被称作当年人们所谓的“职业美人”。”

“她确实生得漂亮，而且是个招人疼的甜姐儿！瞧她这会儿，不正跟迈尔斯·拉什沃思（Miles Rushworth）在一块儿吗？”

说话的人朝那扇敞开的落地长窗扬了扬头。这是一间十八世纪的客厅，顶棚高敞，呈椭圆形。晚饭后，她和另外两位女士正坐在屋里；这天是星期六晚上，在汉普顿太太看来，这个周末聚会正显出一派圆满成功的气象。

长窗外是一方宽阔的石砌露台。露台再往外，是一片开阔的草坪，浸在皎洁的月光里；草坪上有两个人影在缓缓信步——一个是高大的男人，一个是身穿浅色衣裙的窈窕女子。他们正走离这座美丽的古老宅邸——两人都是来这里做客的——朝一条山毛榉林荫道踱去。

===Original===
Mary Hampton went on, speaking not unkindly, but with a certain tartness: “He took her out in his motor after tea, so she might have left him alone after dinner.”
“You oughtn’t to complain, my dear! You told me this morning that you had asked the Lextons this weekend so that they could make friends with your millionaire,” observed Joan Rodney.
She was a sharp-tongued, clever spinster who enjoyed putting her friends right, and telling them home truths. Much was forgiven to Miss Rodney because she was, if sharp-tongued, fundamentally kindhearted.
“My millionaire, as you call him, is one of the finest amateur billiard-players in England. I made Jack get hold of the best of the young ‘pros.’ He could only spare us this evening, and now that all the men, and two of the women, are either playing or watching the play in the billiard-room, Miles is philandering with Ivy Lexton in the garden!”

===Chinese===
玛丽·汉普顿（Mary Hampton）接着说，语气不算刻薄，却带着几分酸意：“喝过下午茶他就开车带她出去兜风了，晚饭之后她总该让他清静清静吧。”

“这可怨不得别人，亲爱的！你今早还跟我说，这回请莱克斯顿夫妇来过周末，就为的让他们结识结识你那位百万富翁，”琼·罗德尼（Joan Rodney）说。

她是个伶牙俐齿、聪明过人的老小姐，最喜欢纠正朋友的错处，把逆耳的实话直说给他们听。不过大家都能原谅罗德尼小姐，因为她嘴上虽然厉害，心地到底是善良的。

“我那位百万富翁——随你怎么叫吧——是全英国最出色的业余台球手之一。我特意让杰克去把年轻一代里最棒的一位“职业球手”请了来。他只有今天晚上抽得出空，可如今呢，男的全体，外加两位女士，不是在台球室里打球就是围着看球，迈尔斯倒跑到花园里跟艾薇·莱克斯顿（Ivy Lexton）调情去了！”

===Original===
“Not philandering, Mary,” observed Lady Flora, smiling. “Mr. Rushworth never philanders.”
“Well! You know what I mean. It’s my fault, of course. I ought to have known that no party would be big enough to hold Ivy Lexton and another attraction. Last time she was here she snatched such a nice boy from his best girl, and stopped, I’m afraid, a proposal.”
Lady Flora looked sorry. A plain woman herself, she admired, without a touch of envy, physical beauty more than she admired anything else in the world.
“I don’t suppose Mrs. Lexton can help attracting men. It’s human nature after all﻿—”

===Chinese===
“不是调情，玛丽，”弗洛拉小姐含笑说道，“拉什沃思先生从不调情。”

“好吧！你明白我的意思。当然，这都怪我。我早该想到，天下没有够大的聚会，容得下艾薇·莱克斯顿之外再有旁人出风头。上回她来，就把一个怪可爱的青年从他心上人身边抢了过来，恐怕还生生断送了一桩求婚呢。”

弗洛拉小姐面有憾色。她自己容貌平常，对天生的丽质却怀着一种全无妒意的倾慕——世上再没有别的什么更让她如此叹赏的了。

“我想莱克斯顿太太招引男人，也是身不由己。这终究是人性嘛——”

===Original===
Quoted Joan Rodney, with a sharp edge to her voice:

> “It’s human nature but, if so, oh!
 

 Isn’t human nature low?”

===Chinese===
琼·罗德尼接口引用道，嗓音里带着尖利的刺儿：

> “人性本是如此；可既然如此，哎呀！

>  人性岂非太低贱？”

===Original===
“Little Ivy isn’t exactly low; at least I hope not,” observed little Ivy’s hostess reflectively. “But I do feel that there’s a curiously soulless quality about her. Though she’s not what people call clever, there’s something baffling about Ivy Lexton. I liked her much better when I first knew her.”
“She mayn’t be what silly people call clever, but she’s plenty of what used to be called ‘nous,’ ” said Miss Rodney drily. “She engineered her stroll with Mr. Rushworth very cleverly tonight. Your husband was determined to get him into the billiard-room﻿—”
“She had a good excuse for that, Joan. As I told you yesterday, Jervis Lexton has been looking out for something to do for a long while.”
Mrs. Hampton turned to her other friend. “It suddenly occurred to me, Flora, that Miles Rushworth, who must have many jobs in his gift, might find Jervis Lexton something to do. Ivy knows that I asked them both for this weekend on purpose that they might meet him. It isn’t easy to get hold of him for this kind of party.”

===Chinese===
“我们的小艾薇倒谈不上低贱；至少我希望不至于，”小艾薇的女主人若有所思地说。“不过我总觉得她身上有一种奇怪的、没有灵魂的气质。虽说她算不上人们所谓的聪明，艾薇·莱克斯顿身上却有些令人捉摸不透的东西。我刚认识她那会儿，要喜欢她得多。”

“她也许不是蠢人们所谓的聪明，可她有的是老话儿说的那种‘乖觉’（nous），”罗德尼小姐干巴巴地说。“今晚她那一趟同拉什沃思先生的散步，就安排得非常巧妙。你丈夫本来铁了心要拉他进台球室的——”

“她那样做自有充分的借口，琼。我昨天不是跟你说了么，杰维斯·莱克斯顿（Jervis Lexton）想找点事做已经好久了。”

汉普顿太太转向另一位朋友。“弗洛拉，我忽然想到，迈尔斯·拉什沃思手里想必掌握着好些职位，兴许能给杰维斯·莱克斯顿找个事儿做。艾薇知道，我这回特意请他们两口子来过周末，就为的让他们跟他见见面。要请动他来赴这种聚会，可不容易。”

===Original===
“Have you known the Lextons long, Mary?” asked Lady Flora.
She felt genuinely interested in Mrs. Jervis Lexton. The quiet, old-fashioned, some would have said very limited, middle-aged widow, and lovely, restless, self-absorbed, and very modern Ivy Lexton, had “made friends.”
“I have known Jervis ever since he was born. His father was a friend of my father’s. But I had not seen him for years till I ran across him, in town, about three months ago. The last time I had seen him was early in the war, when his father had just died, and he had been given a fortnight’s leave. He’s what Jack calls quite a good sort; but it’s bad for a young man to become his own master at twenty. He seems to have married this lovely little thing when he was twenty-two. That’s six years ago.”
“They seem to get on very well,” observed Lady Flora.

===Chinese===
“你认识莱克斯顿夫妇很久了吗，玛丽？”弗洛拉小姐问。

她对杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太当真起了兴致。这位娴静而守旧——有人还会说见识十分有限——的中年寡妇，同这位娇美、不安分、只顾自己而又十足摩登的艾薇·莱克斯顿，竟然“交上了朋友”。

“杰维斯一出世我就认识他。他父亲是我父亲的朋友。可我有好多年没见过他了，直到约莫三个月前，在城里撞见他。上回见面还是大战初期，他父亲刚刚去世，他获准了两周的休假。用杰克的话说，他是个挺不错的人；不过一个年轻人二十岁上就自己当家做主，终究不是好事。他娶这位可爱的小东西，好像是在二十二岁上。那是六年前的事了。”

“他们两口子看样子处得很好，”弗洛拉小姐说。

===Original===
