“I think they do, though I’m afraid they’ve muddled away most of his money in having what Ivy considers a good time. He must have come into a fair fortune, for his father had sold their place just before the war.”
“What fools young people seem to be today﻿—I mean compared to the old days!” exclaimed Joan Rodney harshly.
She went on: “John Oram﻿—you know, Mary, the big solicitor﻿—once told me that of ten men who sell their land at any given time, only two have anything of the purchase-price left at the end of ten years.”
“Jervis Lexton won’t be one of those two men,” said his hostess regretfully. “Ivy told me today that they’re fearfully hard up.”

===Chinese===
“我想是挺好，只怕他的钱大半都让他们糊里糊涂地折腾光了，全花在艾薇所谓的快活日子里。他必定继承过一笔可观的家产，因为大战前夕他父亲刚把家里的田宅卖掉了。”

“如今的年轻人真是糊涂——我是说，跟从前的人比！”琼·罗德尼尖刻地嚷道。

她接着说：“约翰·奥拉姆（John Oram）——就是那位大律师，玛丽，你知道的——有一回告诉我，在随便哪个年月里卖掉地产的十个人当中，到了十年期满，手里还留着些卖地价钱的，不过两个。”

“杰维斯·莱克斯顿怕是挤不进那两个人里头，”他的女主人惋惜地说。

===Original===
“People often say that when it is laughably untrue! It’s the fashion to pretend one’s poor. Mrs. Lexton dresses beautifully. She must spend a great deal of money on her clothes,” interjected Joan Rodney.
“I’m afraid there was no pretence about what Ivy told me this morning. She looked really worried, poor little thing! I do hope she will get something good for Jervis out of Miles Rushworth.”
“She makes most of her frocks herself; it’s so easy nowadays,” said Lady Flora. And then she added: “She was telling me today about her girlhood. Her father failed in business, through no fault of his own, and for a little while she was on the stage﻿—”
“Only a walking-on part in a musical comedy,” observed Joan Rodney, “if what her husband, who strikes me as an honest young fellow, told me is true. However, I’m surprised, even so, that she didn’t do better for herself in what I have heard described as the straight road to the peeresses’ gallery, to say nothing of ‘another place.’ ”

===Chinese===
“人们惯会说这种话，其实假得可笑！装穷如今成了时髦。莱克斯顿太太穿得多讲究。她的衣裳准得花掉好大一笔钱，”琼·罗德尼插嘴道。

“只怕艾薇今早跟我说的话并非装假。她瞧着是真发愁，可怜的小东西！我真心指望她能从迈尔斯·拉什沃思那儿替杰维斯谋到件好事。”

“她那些衣裙多半是自己做的；如今做这个容易得很，”弗洛拉小姐说。接着她又补了一句：“她今天正跟我讲起她少女时候的事。她父亲做买卖破了产——那完全不能怪他——有一小阵子她还在舞台上待过——”

“不过是音乐喜剧里一个走场的龙套，”琼·罗德尼说，“如果她丈夫——我看他是个老实的青年——说的是实话的话。不过即便如此，我还是奇怪，她怎么没在那条人家所谓直通上议院贵妇旁听席的坦途上，给自己谋个更好的前程，更不必说‘另一个去处’了。”

===Original===
“Joan! Joan!” cried Mrs. Hampton deprecatingly.
Miss Rodney got up and came across to where her hostess sat under a heavily-shaded lamp.
She put her left elbow on the marble mantelpiece, and looking down into the other’s now upturned face, “I don’t like your little friend,” she said deliberately. “I’ve been studying her closely ever since she arrived on Thursday afternoon, though she didn’t seem aware of my existence till after lunch today. When I was in America last year, they’d invented a name for that sort of young woman. She’s out, all the time, for what she can get. ‘A gold-digger’﻿—that’s the slang American term for that kind of young person, Mary. I know what I’m talking about.”
“How can you possibly know?”

===Chinese===
“琼！琼！”汉普顿太太连声劝阻道。

罗德尼小姐站起身，走到女主人跟前——她正坐在一盏罩着厚灯罩的灯下。

罗德尼小姐把左肘支在大理石壁炉台上，俯视着对方这时仰起来的脸。“我不喜欢你那位小朋友，”她一字一顿地说。“从她星期四下午进门起，我就在细细打量她，虽然直到今天吃过午饭，她好像才发觉世上有我这么一个人。去年我在美国的时候，人家给这种年轻女人起了个名目。她自始至终，都在一心捞取好处。‘淘金女’（gold-digger）——这是美国俚语里对这类年轻人的叫法，玛丽。我知道自己在说什么。”

“你怎么可能知道呢？”

===Original===
“By instinct, my dear! If I were you I should give pretty Mrs. Lexton a very wide berth.”
And then, rather to the relief of the other two, she exclaimed, “Having done what’s always foolish﻿—that is, said exactly what I think﻿—I’m off to watch the champion billiard-player.”
After she had left the room, Mrs. Hampton said slowly, “It’s sad to hear a good woman, for Joan is really a good woman, say such cruel, unkind things.”
“It’s odd, too, for no one can show more real understanding sympathy when one’s in trouble,” answered Lady Flora in a low voice. She was remembering a time of frightful sorrow in her own life, when Joan Rodney had been one of the few friends whose presence had not jarred on her.

===Chinese===
“凭直觉，亲爱的！我要是你，就对那位漂亮的莱克斯顿太太敬而远之。”

接着，倒让另外两位暗暗松了口气，她扬声说道：“我干完了一桩素来犯傻的事——也就是把心里的想法原原本本说了出来——这就去看冠军球手打球了。”

她出屋之后，汉普顿太太缓缓说道：“听一个好人——琼实实在在是个好人——说出这样刻薄无情的话来，真叫人难受。”

“这也怪，”弗洛拉小姐低声答道，“因为一个人遭了难，再没有人比她更体贴、更懂人心的了。”她想起自己生平一段惨痛的时日，那时候，琼·罗德尼是少数几位待在她身边不让她心烦的朋友之一。

===Original===
“Ah, well! She’s devoted to you. Also, you’re an angel, Flora, so there’s no great merit in being kind to you. What Joan Rodney can’t forgive in another woman is youth, happiness﻿—”
“And, I suppose, beauty,” interjected Lady Flora. “Yet to me there is something so disarming, so pathetic, about Mrs. Lexton.”
“Then Joan has such a poor opinion of human nature,” went on Mrs. Hampton in a vexed tone. “You heard with what delight she quoted that horrid little bit of doggerel. Still, quite between ourselves, Flora, I must admit that, in a sense, she is more right than she knows about Ivy Lexton.”
Lady Flora looked dismayed. “In what way, Mary?”

===Chinese===
“唉，也罢！她一心向着你。再说，你是个天使，弗洛拉，所以待你和善算不得什么功德。琼·罗德尼在别的女人身上不能原谅的，是青春，是幸福——”

“大概还有美貌，”弗洛拉小姐插嘴道。“可是对我说来，莱克斯顿太太身上有那么一种叫人无法设防的东西，那么惹人怜惜。”

“还有，琼把人性看得这样低，”汉普顿太太接着说，语气有些恼恼的。“你听见她念那首难听的歪诗时有多得意。不过，这话只在你我之间说说，弗洛拉，我得承认，从某种意义上说，她对艾薇·莱克斯顿的看法，比她自己意识到的还要正确。”

弗洛拉小姐面露惶惑。“怎么讲呢，玛丽？”

===Original===
“Ivy is very fond of money, or rather of spending it. In fact she is idiotically extravagant. She is dancing mad, and belongs to the two most expensive night clubs in town. It’s her fault that they’ve frittered away a lot of Jervis Lexton’s capital. Also, there’s a side to her, for all her pretty manners, that isn’t pretty at all.”
“How d’you mean?” and the other looked puzzled.
Mrs. Hampton hesitated. Then she smiled a little ruefully. “My maid told me that when Ivy arrived she was quite rude to Annie﻿—you know, my nice old housemaid?﻿—because there was no bottle of scent on her dressing-table! There was one, it seems, last time she was here. It had been left by some visitor﻿—I don’t undertake to provide such luxuries.”
“That doesn’t sound very nice, certainly,” Lady Flora looked naively surprised.

===Chinese===
“艾薇太爱钱了——或者说，太爱花钱了。事实上她挥霍起来简直没脑子。她跳舞跳得着魔，是城里两家花费最昂贵的夜总会的会员。他们把杰维斯·莱克斯顿那么多本金零敲碎打地折腾掉，就是她的缘故。再者，别看她仪态娇美，她还有一面可一点也不娇美。”

“这话怎么讲？”对方露出困惑的神色。

汉普顿太太迟疑了一下，随即自嘲地微微一笑。“我的贴身女仆告诉我，艾薇到的那天，对安妮（Annie）相当无礼——你认识安妮的，我那个和气的老女仆？——就因为她的梳妆台上没有摆香水！说是上回她来的时候倒是有一瓶。那是哪位客人落下的——这类奢侈品我可不负责供应。”

“这听上去确实不怎么样，”弗洛拉小姐显出天真的惊讶。

===Original===
“Then, if I’m to be really honest, my dear, there’s no doubt that one reason why Joan Rodney has taken such a ferocious dislike to Ivy Lexton is owing to the fact that I stupidly told Ivy this morning of Joan’s marvellous bit of luck﻿—I mean of that big legacy from the American cousin. I’m afraid that’s why Ivy, who behaved all yesterday as if Joan hardly existed, began at once to make up to her! But pretty ways are very much lost on our Joan.”
She began to laugh. She really couldn’t help it, remembering the way her friend had received the younger woman’s overtures of friendship.
Lady Flora looked disturbed, for she was one of those rare human beings to whom it is a pain to think ill of anybody.
“After all, Joan’s money is of no good to anyone but herself, Mary? I don’t see why you should suppose poor little Mrs. Lexton made up to her because of that legacy.”

===Chinese===
“再有，倘若要我说实话，亲爱的，那么毫无疑问，琼·罗德尼之所以这样穷凶极恶地厌恶艾薇·莱克斯顿，其中一个缘故就是：今早我蠢头蠢脑地告诉了艾薇琼那桩了不得的好运——我是说她那位美国表亲留下的那笔巨额遗产。恐怕正为这个，艾薇——昨天一整天还装得好像世上根本没有琼这个人似的——立刻就开始巴结她了！只是咱们这位琼，对甜言蜜语丝毫不为所动。”

她笑了起来。想起她那位朋友接受这位年轻太太示好时的那副样子，她实在忍不住。

弗洛拉小姐显得很不安，因为她是那种罕见的人：把人往坏处想，对她自己就是一桩痛苦。

“说到底，琼的钱除了她自己，对谁也没用处呀，玛丽？我不明白你凭什么认定可怜的莱克斯顿太太是冲着那笔遗产去巴结她的。”

===Original===
The other looked at her fixedly.
“Ivy Lexton has a good deal in common with the heroine of Jack’s favourite drawing-room story.”
“What story?” asked Lady Flora. Her host’s rather sly sense of humour had never appealed to her, though they were quite good friends.

===Chinese===
对方直勾勾地望着她。

“艾薇·莱克斯顿跟杰克最得意的那则客厅笑话里的女主角，大有相似之处。”

“什么笑话？”弗洛拉小姐问。男主人那种有点促狭的幽默感，她从来不怎么欣赏，尽管他们交情很不错。

===Original===
“The story of the lady who said to her husband, ‘Oh, do let’s go and see them; they’re so rich!’ to be met with the answer, ‘My dear, I would if it was catching!’ ”
Lady Flora looked a little puzzled. “He was quite right. Money is not catching, though I suppose most people wish it were.”
“A great many people are convinced that it is, Flora, and our little Ivy is among them. I’m sure she feels that if she rubs herself up against it close enough, a little will certainly come off. And I’m not sure, in her case, that she’s not right!”
But Lady Flora could be obstinate in her mild way.

===Chinese===
“就是那位太太的故事呀。她对丈夫说：‘哦，咱们去拜访拜访他们吧，他们多有钱啊！’结果得到的回答是：‘亲爱的，钱要是能沾上人，我就去！’”

弗洛拉小姐显得有点迷惑。“他说得挺对。钱是不会沾上人的，不过我猜多数人都巴望着能沾上。”

“许许多多的人却深信钱能沾上人，弗洛拉，咱们的小艾薇就是其中之一。我敢肯定她觉得，只要往钱跟前贴得够紧，就准能蹭下一点来！而且就她而言，我还真不敢说她想错了！”

不过弗洛拉小姐也会以她那温和的方式固执己见。

===Original===
“I like Mrs. Lexton,” she said gently. “I’m going to call on her when we’re all in town again. She’s promised to take me to a nice quiet night club. I’ve always longed to see one. I want my sister-in-law, I mean Jenny, to know her. Jenny loves young people. She gives amusing little dances﻿—”
“I think you’ll make a mistake if you introduce Ivy to the Duchess.”
“I don’t see why, my dear? After all, Mary, your little friend has been very sweet to me, and that though she knows I’m really poor.”
The other woman gave a quick look at her friend. Sometimes she thought Flora Desmond too good, too simple, even for human nature’s daily food.

===Chinese===
“我喜欢莱克斯顿太太，”她轻声说。“等大家再回到城里，我要去拜访她。她答应带我去看一家又好又清静的夜总会。我一直巴望见识见识呢。我还要让我弟媳——就是珍妮（Jenny）——认识认识她。珍妮喜欢年轻人。她常办些有趣的小舞会——”

“我看你要是把艾薇引见给公爵夫人，可就办错事了。”

“我不明白为什么，亲爱的？说到底，玛丽，你这位小朋友待我一直很好，何况她是明知道我实在穷得很的。”

对方飞快地瞥了她的朋友一眼。有时候她想，弗洛拉·德斯蒙德这般善良、这般单纯，拿来充当凡人俗性的日常食粮，未免太过了。

## V / 五
===Original===

“Look at lucky Olive Larnoch. A month ago she didn’t know where to turn for sixpence!”

Ivy Lexton, and one of her young married women friends, Janet Horley, were lunching together at the Embassy Club.

Every place in the great room was occupied. At the next table an American diplomat was being lunched by one of the younger Ministers of the Crown; and close by a popular actor-manager was entertaining a pretty young duchess. Two sisters, who had just leapt into musical comedy fame, were laughing at the top of their voices, while being gaily chaffed by their host, an elderly peer who had entertained two generations of charming women by the daring quality of his wit.

Olive Larnoch? Ivy gazed eagerly across at a couple sitting at right angles from where she sat herself.

“Look at her string of pearls studded with huge diamonds! They’re all real!” went on Mrs. Horley excitedly. “As for the emerald Jock Larnoch gave her the day they became engaged, it’s worth five thousand pounds﻿—”

===Chinese===

“瞧瞧走运的奥莉芙·拉诺克（Olive Larnoch）吧。一个月前，她连六便士都没处张罗去！”

艾薇·莱克斯顿（Ivy Lexton）正同她一位年轻的已婚女友珍妮特·霍利（Janet Horley）一起，在使馆俱乐部（Embassy Club）用午餐。

大厅里座无虚席。邻桌上，一位美国外交官正受着一位年轻阁员的宴请；近旁，一位当红的演员兼剧院经理正在款待一位年轻貌美的公爵夫人。一对刚刚在音乐喜剧界声名鹊起的姐妹放开了嗓门大笑，招待她们的主人——一位凭谈吐的泼辣大胆讨了两代可爱女人欢心的年迈勋爵——正乐呵呵地打趣她们。

奥莉芙·拉诺克？艾薇急切地朝斜对着自己座位的一男一女望去，那两人坐的位子恰与她成一个直角。

“瞧她那串缀满大颗钻石的珍珠项链！颗颗都是真的！”霍利太太（Mrs. Horley）越说越起劲，“至于乔克·拉诺克（Jock Larnoch）在他们订婚那天送她的那颗绿宝石，值五千英镑呢——”

===Original===

“I’m sure I’ve seen her before,” exclaimed Ivy.

“Of course you must have often seen her, in the old days, when she was Olive Ryde, a war widow without a bob﻿—”

“﻿—and a stocking-shop in North Bolton Street?”

“You’ve got it in one! And her stockings always laddered, too. Well, one evening, she met a Scotch man of business here at the Embassy, named Jock Larnoch. He’d never been in a nightclub before, so I suppose it went to his head! I happen to know the people who brought him here, and the funny thing is that they hadn’t an idea he was made of money. They just thought him comfortably off. Yet his first love-gift to Olive was a Baby Rolls﻿—she didn’t know what to do with it, poor dear!”

===Chinese===

“我肯定从前见过她。”艾薇脱口说道。

“你当然常见她啦，早先她还是奥莉芙·莱德（Olive Ryde）的时候——一个身无分文的战争寡妇——”

“——外加北博尔顿街（North Bolton Street）的一爿丝袜铺子？”

“一猜就中！她的丝袜还老是抽丝呢。喏，有一天晚上，她在这儿使馆俱乐部遇上了个苏格兰生意人，叫乔克·拉诺克。这人从前从没进过夜总会，我瞧他是被这地方冲昏了头！领他上这儿来的那几位我恰好认识，有意思的是，他们压根儿不知道他腰缠万贯，只当他家境宽裕。可他送给奥莉芙的头一件爱情信物，竟是一辆小号劳斯莱斯——可怜的人儿，她都不知道该拿它怎么办才好！”

===Original===

Ivy gazed with absorbed interest at the fortunate bride of the Scotch millionaire. How marvellous it must be to have everything one wants, including an adoring husband! She sighed a quick, bitter little secret sigh. The sight of this fortunate young woman had brought back to her poignant memories and a sudden realisation of what her life might be now, had she been, a few weeks ago, what Miles Rushworth called “free.”

“Is that nice-looking man her spouse?”

“Heavens, no! That’s Bob Crickle, who wrote the book of *T’wee-t’we*. Jock Larnoch spends every other week ‘at the works’; there never was such a lucky girl as Olive!”

Again, with a sensation almost of despair, Ivy thought of Rushworth, and of all that he might have meant in her life by now if he hadn’t been so﻿—so old-fashioned and queer in his ideas.

===Chinese===

艾薇全神贯注地端详着这位苏格兰百万富翁的幸运新娘。想要什么就有什么，外带一位百般疼爱自己的丈夫，那该多美啊！她飞快地、苦涩地、暗自叹了口气。这个幸运的年轻女人让她想起了种种锥心的旧事，也让她猛然醒悟：几个星期前，她若是当上了迈尔斯·拉什沃思（Miles Rushworth）所说的那种“自由身”，如今的日子会是怎样一副光景。

“那个相貌堂堂的男人是她丈夫吗？”

“天哪，不是！那是鲍勃·克里克尔（Bob Crickle），音乐喜剧《特威-特威》（*T’wee-t’we*）的编剧。乔克·拉诺克每隔一个星期就得待在厂里；天底下再没有奥莉芙这么有福气的姑娘了！”

艾薇又几乎带着绝望想起了拉什沃思，想起倘若不是他观念那样——那样古板、那样别扭，他此刻本可以在她生命里意味着的一切。

===Original===

The Lextons had been settled down in London for nearly two months, and Jervis was going to Rushworth’s City office each morning. As for Ivy, she was once more a popular member of the happy-go-lucky, while for the most part financially solid, set with whom she had danced, played bridge, lunched, and supped through life, in the days when she and her husband were still living on what remained of Jervis’s fortune.

But woman does not live by amusement alone. Ivy loved being loved, so she had “made it up” with Roger Gretorex.

Rushworth was far away, and though he wrote to her by every mail, his letters, as she sometimes pettishly told herself, might have been read aloud at Charing Cross. So it was that, though she had really done with Gretorex, she still went, now and again, to Ferry Place, but far less often than in the days when she had been utterly down on her luck, and at odds with Fate.

And yet, though the Lextons’ troubles seemed over, black care was again beginning to dog Ivy’s light footsteps, for she was once more what she called, to herself, very hard up.

===Chinese===

莱克斯顿夫妇在伦敦安顿下来已近两个月，杰维斯每天早晨去拉什沃思在伦敦城的写字楼上班。至于艾薇，她又成了那个乐天随缘、大体上手头殷实的圈子里招人喜欢的成员——想当年她和丈夫还靠杰维斯那份家产的余烬过活时，就是同这班人跳舞、打桥牌、共进午餐和宵夜，一路厮混过来的。

可是，女人并不能只靠寻欢作乐活着。艾薇需要被人爱着，于是她跟罗杰·格雷托雷克斯（Roger Gretorex）“言归于好”了。

拉什沃思远在海外，虽说每班邮件都给她来信，可那些信，正如她有时赌气对自己说的，拿到查令十字（Charing Cross）当众念出来也使得。就这样，她虽说不折不扣地跟格雷托雷克斯了结了，却仍旧隔三差五地去费里街（Ferry Place），只是去得远不如当初她彻底倒霉、与命运失和的那些日子勤了。

然而莱克斯顿夫妇的麻烦看似了结，那黑色的忧愁却又开始悄悄缠上艾薇轻盈的脚步，因为她再度落入了她私下自认的境地：手头窘迫极了。

===Original===

True, the couple were now living in what appeared to Ivy’s husband extreme comfort, and even luxury. Not only was their flat one of the best in the fine block called the Duke of Kent Mansion, charmingly furnished; but an excellent cook, and a good day-maid had been left there by Miles Rushworth’s cousins. So what might have been called the Lextons’ home-life ran as if on wheels.

From the moment, however, that Ivy had come back to London, secure in the knowledge that her husband was now earning a thousand pounds a year, paid monthly, she had again fallen into the way of buying, or, better still, of ordering on account, any pretty costly trifle, any becoming frock or hat, that took her fancy. She also, in a way that seemed modest to herself, had at once begun to entertain.

It was such fun to give lively little luncheon parties to her women friends﻿—lunch being followed as often as not by bridge! One, sometimes even two, bridge tables would be set out in the attractive drawing-room and, in due course, a bountiful tea would be served by the smiling day-maid, for those of Ivy’s guests who were not afraid of getting fat.

===Chinese===

诚然，这对夫妇如今过着的日子，在艾薇丈夫看来已是舒适至极，甚至称得上奢华。他们那套公寓坐落在名为肯特公爵大厦（Duke of Kent Mansion）的堂堂大楼里，是楼中数一数二的好单元，陈设雅致悦目；迈尔斯·拉什沃思的表亲们还留下了一位手艺上乘的厨娘和一个不错的日班女仆。因此，莱克斯顿家的家庭生活，可以说运转得像上了轮子一样顺溜。

然而，艾薇一回到伦敦，心里笃定丈夫如今一年挣一千英镑、按月支薪，便又养成了老习惯：凡是入眼的漂亮玩意儿，不论价钱多贵——一件合身的连衣裙也好，一顶帽子也好——说买就买，更妙的还是记账赊购。她还立刻开始请客宴饮起来，那规模在她自己看来还算是节制的。

请几位女友来吃顿热闹的小午餐，多有意思——午餐过后十有八九还要打桥牌！那间赏心悦目的客厅里会摆开一桌、有时甚至两桌桥牌，到时候，笑吟吟的日班女仆还会端上丰盛的茶点，招待艾薇那些不怕发胖的客人。

===Original===

The good-natured old cook had not been used to so much work, and she had very soon declared, not unreasonably, that she must have extra help in the kitchen.

Lexton, who was rather pathetically anxious “to make good,” always went down to the City each morning by the Underground. But he came back by omnibus, and he invariably dropped in at his club on his way home, and, as he was an openhearted fellow, he often asked one of his new business acquaintances to drop in too. That, also, meant entertaining, but on a far more modest scale than that in which Ivy indulged.

Though Mrs. Jervis Lexton had learnt long ago the fine art of living on credit, there are a great many things which even in London a prosperous young couple with a good address cannot obtain, as it were, for nothing. Each week many pounds slipped through pretty, popular Ivy’s fingers, and she honestly could not have told you how or why. So it was inevitable that she should again begin to feel short of money﻿—short, even, of petty cash.

Often she told herself that it was maddening to feel that if Rushworth were in England she could almost certainly have had all the money she needed, and that without too great a sacrifice of her pride, or, what was far more important, his good opinion of her. Just before leaving for South Africa he had given her a hundred pounds as “a birthday gift.” How good he was, how generous! Her heart thrilled with real gratitude when she thought of Miles Rushworth.

===Chinese===

那位好脾气的老厨娘向来没做过这么多的活计，不多久便宜布——这倒也不算无理取闹——厨房里非得添个帮手不可。

莱克斯顿一心焦切地想要“干出个名堂”——说来有点可怜——每天早晨总是乘地铁进城。傍晚回来却坐公共汽车，回家路上总要到俱乐部里转一转；他又是个好客的热心肠，常拉上一位生意场上的新相识一同去坐坐。这同样意味着待客开销，只是比起艾薇纵情铺张的排场，简省得多了。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太虽然早就学会了赊账度日的高妙手艺，可在伦敦，一对门面体面的阔绰年轻夫妇，也有许许多多东西是没法白来的。一个星期又一个星期，好些英镑从漂亮讨喜、人缘又好的艾薇指缝间溜走，让她自己说，她还真说不出是怎么溜走的、又为什么溜走。于是不可避免地，她又渐渐觉得缺钱花了——缺的甚至只是零钱。

她时常暗自嘀咕，一想起来就气闷：倘若拉什沃思人在英国，她要多少钱几乎准能到手，而且既不至于太折损她的自尊，更不至于——这才要紧得多——折损他对她的敬重。动身去南非之前，他给了她一百英镑，算作“一份生日礼物”。他心地多好，为人多慷慨啊！一想起迈尔斯·拉什沃思，她心里便涌起真真切切的感激。

===Original===

---

Late in the same day that his wife had lunched at the Embassy Club, Lexton, who was going out to what he called a stag party that evening, came back from the City to find an unpleasantly threatening letter, this time from a tailor to whom he had owed for years a huge bill, and who had evidently just heard of his newfound prosperity.

For once Ivy’s husband looked ruffled and cross, and she, also for once, felt very angry indeed. Jervis had begun, so she told herself with rage, to put on airs, just because he had a job﻿—a job that she was too clever and tactful ever to remind him was entirely owing to her friendship with his employer.

At last he left the flat and, with a feeling of relief, she went off, too, by omnibus, to the tiny house where Roger Gretorex lived and practised his profession. It was in what might have been called a slum, though each of the mid-Victorian, two-storied, cottage-like dwellings were now inhabited by decent working people and their families.

Ivy had not been to No. 6 Ferry Place for nearly a fortnight, and her lover had written her a long, reproachful letter, imploring her to come and see him there, if for only a few moments. It made him feel so wretched, so he wrote, never to see her now, except in the company of people and in surroundings which filled him with contemptuous dislike, or, in a sense worse still, only in the presence of her husband.

===Chinese===

---

妻子在使馆俱乐部用午餐的当天傍晚，莱克斯顿晚上要去赴一场他所谓的“男人局”，从伦敦城回来时，却发现了一封措辞威胁、令人不快的信。这一回写信的是个裁缝，他欠了人家一笔多年的巨额账单，而人家显然刚听说他新近发了迹。

艾薇的丈夫难得地面露愠色、火气冲冲，而她也是难得地当真动了大怒。杰维斯倒摆起架子来了，她愤愤地暗想——就因为他有了份差事！可这份差事，全亏她同他东家的交情才谋来的，这一点她聪明乖觉得很，从来不肯点破。

他总算离开了公寓，她如释重负，也坐公共汽车出门，去罗杰·格雷托雷克斯居住行医的那所小房子。那地方本可以算作贫民窟，只是那一排排维多利亚中期的两层小屋，如今住的都还是体面的工人和他们的家眷。

艾薇已近两个星期没去费里街6号（No. 6 Ferry Place）了，她的情人便写来一封长信，字里行间满是怨责，恳求她到那儿去看看他，哪怕只待几分钟也好。他信上说，如今总也见不着她单独一面，见她不是在那些他所鄙夷厌恶的人群和场合里，便是——某种意义上更糟——只在她丈夫在场的时候，这叫他心里苦透了。

===Original===

When everything was going well with Ivy Lexton, she felt bored, often even irritated, with Roger Gretorex and his great love for her. But the moment she was under the weather and worried, as she was again beginning to be, then she found it a comfort to be with a man who not only worshipped her, but who never wanted her to make any effort to amuse or flatter him, as did all the other men with whom she was now once more thrown in contact.

So it was that this late afternoon, immediately after Jervis had left the flat, she telephoned and told the enraptured Gretorex that as she happened to have this evening free, she would come and have dinner with him at Ferry Place.

And yet, as she sat in the almost empty omnibus on her way to Westminster, her heart and her imagination were full of Miles Rushworth, and not once did she even throw a fleeting thought to the man she was going to see. Gretorex had become to Ivy Lexton what she had once heard a friend of hers funnily describe as a kind of “Stepney” to her husband. Sometimes she felt that she really preferred Jervis to Roger. Jervis was so kindly, easygoing, unexacting.

Still tonight she felt cross with Jervis, because of the scrap they had had over the tailor’s bill, so the thought of secret revenge was sweet.

===Chinese===

凡是日子顺遂的时候，艾薇·莱克斯顿就觉得罗杰·格雷托雷克斯和他那份炽烈的爱情乏味，甚至常常惹她心烦。可一旦她情绪低落、心事重重——如今她渐渐又成了这样——那么守着一个男人便成了安慰：这个男人不但崇拜她，还从不指望她费心取悦讨好，不像她如今重新周旋的那些男人，个个都要。

就这样，这天傍晚，杰维斯前脚刚离开公寓，她后脚便挂了电话，告诉喜出望外的格雷托雷克斯：今晚她恰好得空，要去费里街同他共进晚餐。

然而当她坐在几乎空荡的公共汽车上往威斯敏斯特去时，心里、想象里满满当当都是迈尔斯·拉什沃思，对要去见的那个男人，连一个转瞬的念头都不曾抛去过。格雷托雷克斯之于艾薇·莱克斯顿，正如她曾听一位女友打趣形容的，好比是她丈夫的一只“斯蒂普尼”（Stepney，备用车轮）。有时她觉得，比起罗杰来，自己还真更喜欢杰维斯。杰维斯多么和气、随和、不苛求人。

不过今晚她还在为裁缝账单那场口角生杰维斯的气，所以一想到背地里的报复，心里便甜滋滋的。

===Original===

But the image securely throned in her inmost heart was that of Miles Rushworth.

The knowledge that Rushworth, a man possessed of great, to her imagination limitless, wealth, was loving her, longing for her, and yet, owing to his oversensitive, absurdly scrupulous, conscience, hopelessly out of her reach, awoke in Ivy Lexton a feeling of fierce, passionate exasperation.

At last she stepped lightly out of the omnibus, the conductor, and an old gentleman who had been her only fellow-passenger, eagerly assisting her. She smiled at them both. Even the most trifling tribute to her beauty always gave her a touch of genuine pleasure. She was looking very pretty tonight in a charming frock, and in her hand she held the curious little bolster bag which Rushworth had bought for her at Dieppe.

===Chinese===

然而在她内心深处稳稳踞于宝座之上的，还是迈尔斯·拉什沃思的身影。

拉什沃思家赀巨万——在她的想象里简直家财无限——这样一个男人爱着她、渴念着她，却由于他那过分敏感、荒唐地一丝不苟的良心，叫她永远够不着边；这份认知在艾薇·莱克斯顿心头撩起一股狂暴而炽烈的愤懑。

终于她轻盈地跨下公共汽车，售票员，还有车上唯一同她一路的老先生，都殷勤地扶了她一把。她朝两人嫣然一笑。哪怕是最微不足道的殷勤，只要冲着她的美貌来，也总能给她一点真切的愉悦。今晚她穿一身迷人的连衣裙，出落得十分俏丽，手里拎着那只古雅的小长枕包——拉什沃思在迪耶普（Dieppe）给她买的。

===Original===

---

Eight o’clock boomed from Big Ben, and Roger Gretorex, his arm round Ivy’s shoulder, led her into the tiny dining-room, where had been prepared in haste an attractive little meal. She had been what the man who loved her with so devoted and absorbing a passion, called “kind,” and he felt happy and at peace.

After they had finished dinner they sat on at table for a while, and, as she looked across at him, Ivy told herself that her lover was indeed a splendid-looking man﻿—a man many a woman would envy her.

“Sometimes,” he said in a low voice, “I dream such a wonderful dream, my dearest. I dreamt it last night﻿—”

She looked at him roguishly. “Tell me your dream!”

===Chinese===

---

大本钟（Big Ben）敲响八点，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯搂着艾薇的肩膀，引她走进那间小小的餐室，一顿匆匆备下却颇为可口的家常饭正等着他们。她方才的表现，用那个以一片痴情深爱着她的男人的话说，是“体贴”极了；他只觉得幸福而安宁。

吃罢晚餐，两人又在桌边坐了一会儿。艾薇望着对面的他，心里暗想：她这个情人确是个仪表堂堂的男人——多少女人要眼红她的福气呢。

“有时候，”他低声说，“我会做一个顶美顶美的梦，我最亲爱的。昨儿夜里我就梦见了——”

她淘气地望着他。“讲给我听听，你的梦！”

===Original===

“I dreamt that you were free, and that we were married, you and I﻿—”

She made no answer to that remark, only shook her head, a little pettishly. For one thing, she always felt a trifle cross, as well as bored, when Gretorex talked in what she called to herself a sloppy, sentimental way. Could he seriously suppose that, if she had the good fortune to be what he called “free,” she would marry a poverty-stricken doctor who was forced to live and work in a slum? He evidently did suppose that; and the fact that he did so made her feel uncomfortable.

“I don’t set out to be particularly good, Roger, but I do think it awfully wrong to talk like that!”

He said slowly, “I agree, it is.”

===Chinese===

“我梦见你成了自由身，梦见你我两人结了婚——”

对这话她不作一声，只赌气似的轻轻摇了摇头。一来，每逢格雷托雷克斯用她私下叫作“腻腻歪歪、多愁善感”的腔调说话，她总觉得有点气恼，又觉得乏味。难道他当真以为，她若有幸成了他所谓的“自由身”，会去嫁一个穷得叮当响、只得在贫民窟里安身行医的医生？他显然就是这么想的；而他居然这么想，倒叫她心里不自在了。

“我倒没打算做什么特别端正的好人，罗杰，可我实在觉得，这么说话太不应该了！”

他缓缓说道：“我也承认，是不应该。”

===Original===

“It makes me feel I oughtn’t to come and see you like this, in your own house. Jervis would be very much put out if he knew I ever came here.”

Gretorex, wincing inwardly, made no answer to that observation. Sometimes this woman, who was all his life, would say something that made him experience a violent feeling of recoil.

She had got up as she spoke, and with a sensation of relief she put on her hat. It was still early, and she had suddenly remembered an amusing bachelor girl named Judy Swinston, who lived not far from here, in Queen Anne’s Mansions. Judy had said that she was always at home after dinner on Thursdays, so why shouldn’t she, Ivy, go along there now? She could telephone from a call office to find out if it was really true that the Bohemian crowd who formed Judy Swinston’s circle didn’t bother to dress.

In some ways Ivy Lexton was very conventional. She would have disliked making part of any gathering which could be called a party, in her present day-frock and walking shoes, charming as were both the frock and the shoes. It was a perfect St. Martin’s summer evening, more like June than the first of November.

===Chinese===

“这么一说，倒叫我觉得不该这样上你家里来看你了。杰维斯要是知道我来过这儿，准会大大地不痛快。”

格雷托雷克斯心里一缩，对这话却没接腔。有时候，这个被他当作整个生命的女人，会说出几句叫他猛然生出退缩之意的话来。

说话间她已经站起身来，如释重负地戴上了帽子。时候还早，她忽然想起一个风趣的独身姑娘，叫朱迪·斯温斯顿（Judy Swinston），就住得离这儿不远，在女王安妮大厦（Queen Anne’s Mansions）。朱迪说过，每逢星期四晚饭后她一律在家，那么她艾薇这会儿何不就上那儿去？她可以先到公用电话亭挂个电话，问问围着朱迪·斯温斯顿转的那伙波希米亚朋友当真不拘衣着打扮的传闻，是真是假。

在某些方面，艾薇·莱克斯顿拘泥得很。穿着此刻这身白天的连衣裙和走路鞋——衣裙也好，鞋子也好，都一样迷人——去混进任何一个称得上聚会的场合，她是不乐意的。这是个再好不过的圣马丁小阳春（St. Martin’s summer）之夜，与其说像十一月初，不如说像六月天。

===Original===

“You’re not going yet?” asked Gretorex, in a tone almost of anguish.

“Jervis said he’d try and be back by half-past ten, so I knew I’d have to be home early.”

“I see. All right. You won’t mind my walking with you a little way?”

And then she turned and faced him, angry at his obtuseness. How utterly selfish men were!

===Chinese===

“你这就走了？”格雷托雷克斯问道，声调里几乎带着痛苦。

“杰维斯说了，他尽量十点半以前赶回来，所以我知道自己得早点儿到家。”

“明白了。好吧。我陪你走一小段路，你不介意吧？”

这时她转过身来直视着他，为他这般迟钝而生怒。男人们是多么彻底地自私啊！

===Original===

“I should mind﻿—mind very much indeed! Whenever we’ve left the place together, I’ve always felt uncomfortable. Taxis go all sorts of ways nowadays﻿—just to make their fares bigger, I suppose. The other day a taxi brought Jervis and me down close here, past the end of this street, though it was quite out of our way. I should hate it, if he met us walking together at this time of night. He would think it so queer.”

Again he said sorely, “I see. All right.”

Suddenly there came the sound of raucous cries echoing down Ferry Place.

“… Verdict in the Branksome Case.﻿ ﻿… All the winners!”

===Chinese===

“我介意——当真非常介意！每逢我们俩一道离开这地方，我总是不自在。如今的出租车净绕道儿走——我琢磨着，无非想多挣几个车钱。前两天就有一辆出租车，拉着杰维斯和我愣是拐到这附近，打这条街的街口过，其实跟我们顺路与否全不相干。要是让他这么晚撞见我们俩走在一块儿，我才恨呢。他会觉得蹊跷死了。”

他又委屈地说了一遍：“明白了。好吧。”

忽然，一阵沙哑的叫卖声顺着费里街回荡过来。

“……布兰克瑟姆案（Branksome Case）判决揭晓……赛马赢家全名单！”

===Original===

“I thought they weren’t allowed to cry papers now?” said Ivy, as the shouts drew nearer and nearer.

“They cry them down here. As for the Branksome Case, to my mind the verdict is a foregone conclusion. The man will hang, and they’ll let the woman off﻿—though she ought to hang too!”

“A lot of people were talking about the Branksome mystery where I was lunching today,” exclaimed Ivy. “I knew nothing about it, so I felt rather a fool. The truth is, I’m not a bit interested in murders, Roger. I think it’s morbid to want to know about such things.”

“Do you, darling? Then I’m afraid I’m morbid. This Branksome Case is of peculiar interest to every medical man, owing to the simple fact that there is a great deal of secret poisoning going on nowadays.”

===Chinese===

“我还以为如今不许叫卖报纸了呢？”艾薇说，喊声已经越来越近。

“这一带照旧叫卖。至于布兰克瑟姆案，依我看，判决早成定局。男的要被绞死，女的会开脱——虽说她也该绞！”

“今天我吃午饭那儿，好多人都在议论布兰克瑟姆这件疑案。”艾薇嚷道，“我一点儿内情都不晓得，只觉得自己傻透了。实话说吧，罗杰，我对凶案一点儿也不感兴趣。我觉得，人要是一门心思打听这种事，就有点病态。”

“你当真这么想吗，亲爱的？那我恐怕就是个病态的人了。这桩布兰克瑟姆案，每个行医的人都格外关心，原因也简单：如今暗地里下毒的事太多了。”

===Original===

“What a horrid idea!” And Ivy Lexton did indeed think it very horrid.

“Horrid, no doubt. But I’m afraid unquestionably true. In fact I heard the question put only the other day, as to what a doctor ought to do if he suspects anything of the sort is going on?”

She looked at him with a certain curiosity. “What would you do, Roger?”

“I’ve never been able to make up my mind. Of course, this Branksome story was complicated by the fact that two were in it﻿—a husband and a wife. They’d got everything they could out of the woman’s lover, so they made up their minds to do away with him. They were awfully clever, and it’s a marvel they were ever found out.”

===Chinese===

“多吓人的想法！”艾薇·莱克斯顿也确实觉得这念头怪吓人的。

“吓人是不假。可恐怕千真万确。就在前几天，我还听人提出过这么个问题：倘若医生疑心有这等事情发生，他该怎么办？”

她带着几分好奇望着他。“换了你会怎么办，罗杰？”

“我自己也一直拿不定主意。当然，布兰克瑟姆这桩案子还夹缠着一层：里头是两个人——一对夫妻。他们把那女人的情人身上能榨的都榨干了，便打定主意把他除掉。两口子聪明极了，事情居然败露，真算得上奇迹。”

===Original===

“How did they do it?” she asked, eager at last.

“With arsenic﻿—fly papers.”

“Fly papers?”

He laughed. “Wonderful what people will do sometimes, isn’t it? Steeping fly papers in water has long been a common way of ridding oneself of a tiresome husband. There’s arsenic in almost everything we use﻿—at least, that’s what’s said.”

===Chinese===

“他们是怎么下的手？”她终于来了兴致，问道。

“用砒霜（arsenic）——灭蝇纸。”

“灭蝇纸？”

他笑了。“人有时候干得出的事，真是妙不可言，你说是不是？把灭蝇纸泡在水里，早就成了除掉碍事丈夫的寻常法子。我们日常用的东西里头，差不多样样都含着砒霜——至少，人家是这么说的。”

===Original===

“Arsenic?” Ivy pronounced the word very carefully. It was a new word in her limited vocabulary.

He smiled across at her. Every moment of her presence was precious to him, so he talked on, eager too. “There’s plenty of the stuff in my surgery, at any rate. It’s a splendid tonic, as well as a poison.”

“What a funny thing!” and she smiled at him, apparently rather amused at the notion.

Looking back, for even Ivy Lexton looked back now and again to certain crucial moments of her life, she realised it must have been at that very moment that a certain as yet vague and formless plan slipped into her mind.

===Chinese===

“砒霜？”艾薇一字一顿地念出这个词。在她那不多的词汇里，这是个新词。

他含笑望着她。她在跟前的一分一秒于他都珍贵无比，于是他接着往下说，也来了兴致。“反正我这诊室里这种东西有的是。它既是毒药，也是一味顶好的补药。”

“多有趣的事儿！”她冲他一笑，这念头似乎让她觉得挺好玩。

事后回望——就连艾薇·莱克斯顿也偶尔回顾自己一生中那几个紧要关头——她意识到，必定正是在那一刻，某个尚且模糊混沌的盘算，悄悄溜进了她的脑子。

===Original===

“As a matter of fact,” Gretorex smiled back at her, “I’ve got to make up and send off this very evening a mixture which will contain arsenic﻿—”

“I must be off now,” Ivy said, a thought regretfully.

They walked down the short passage, and through the two doors which separated the house from the surgery. Once there he turned up the light. Anything﻿—anything to keep her a few moments longer in his company!

He went quickly across from the door to the left corner of the bare, low-ceilinged room, now his surgery, which had once been an outhouse. There he unlocked the cupboard where he kept his dangerous drugs, and lifted down a jar on which was printed on a red label the word “**Arsenic**.” Placing it on a deal table above which was a hanging bookcase, he exclaimed, “If you would like to see, darling, what﻿—”

===Chinese===

“说起来，”格雷托雷克斯也报以微笑，“就在今晚我还得调配好、寄出去一剂药水，里头就要用到砒霜——”

“我这可真得走了。”艾薇说，语气里带着一丝歉意。

两人走过短短的过道，穿过隔开住宅与诊室的那两道门。一进去他便把灯拧亮。什么都行——只要能让她在他身边多待上一会儿，什么都行！

他快步从门口走到这间光秃秃的低顶房间的左角——如今充作他的诊室，原先是间外屋。他在那儿打开存放烈性药品的橱柜的锁，取下一只罐子，红标签上印着一个词：“**砒霜**”。他把罐子搁在一张松木桌上，桌上方的墙上挂着一口书架，随即喊道：“你要是想瞧瞧，亲爱的，这——”

===Original===

And then there came a thunderous knock at the front door of the tiny house.

“Wait one moment! Don’t go yet, dearest,” he said hastily. “I won’t be a minute!”

He rushed away, though even in his haste he did not forget to shut both the doors which separated the surgery from the rest of the house.

Ivy Lexton gave a quick look round the sordid-looking stone-flagged walled space, through which her eager little feet had so often carried her last winter, at a time when she had been in love, really in love, with Roger Gretorex.

===Chinese===

就在这时，小房子的前门响起擂鼓般的敲门声。

“稍等片刻！先别走，最亲爱的。”他急忙说，“我去去就来！”

他奔了出去，虽是匆忙，却仍没忘记把隔开诊室与房子其余部分的那两道门都关上。

艾薇·莱克斯顿飞快地环顾这间四壁萧然、石板铺地的小屋。去年冬天，她那双急切的小脚曾多少次把她带进这里——那时候她正爱着罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，是真真正正地爱着。

===Original===

She noted that there was no blind to the little square window. But, even in the unlikely event of anyone looking in through that window, no one standing outside could see, while she stood by the table, what she was doing, or was about to do.

Standing very still, she listened. From the consulting-room at the other end of the passage there came the sound of voices, raised in argument.

Hesitatingly she took up the jar Gretorex had placed on the table. Then she looked at it with avid curiosity. How strange and exciting to know that Death was in that jar﻿—prisoned, but ready to escape and become the servant of any quick-witted, determined human being.

When, at last, she put the jar down again, she noticed what she had not seen till now, that a glass spoon lay on the table.

===Chinese===

她注意到那扇小方窗上没有窗帘。不过，纵然万一真有人往窗里张望，只要她站在桌边，外头的人也瞧不见她在做什么，或打算做什么。

她一动不动地站着，侧耳倾听。过道另一头的诊察室里传来人声，提高了嗓门，在争执。

她迟疑地拿起格雷托雷克斯搁在桌上的那只罐子，继而带着贪婪的好奇打量它。一想到死神就装在这只罐子里——囚禁着，却随时准备脱身而出，去侍奉任何一个脑子快、心肠狠的主儿，这是多么离奇、多么叫人兴奋啊。

等她终于把罐子重新放下，才注意到一样方才没瞧见的东西：桌上放着一把玻璃匙。

===Original===

Once more she looked round the bare surgery. Once more she listened intently, only to hear again Gretorex’s voice, far away, raised in argument.

All at once and in feverish haste she began unscrewing the top of the jar. Once this was achieved, she pressed the jewelled top of her bolster bag, and as it sprang open, she took her powder puff out of its pochette. Then, taking the glass spoon off the table, with its help she began shaking into the little white leather-lined pocket a quantity of the powder which she knew to be a deadly poison.

After snapping-to the bag, she replaced its cap on the jar labelled “Arsenic” and screwed it tight. Then she stepped back from the table a little way, and stood quite still, thinking of what she had just done.

Why had she done that﻿—to herself she called it “funny” thing? Deep in her heart she knew quite well the answer to her own wordless question. But she did not admit her purpose, even to herself.

===Chinese===

她又一次环顾这间空落落的诊室，又一次凝神细听，听见的仍旧只是远处格雷托雷克斯提高嗓门争执的声音。

冷不丁地，她发起狂似的匆忙拧起罐盖来。盖子一拧开，她便按上长枕包那镶着珠宝的按扣，包盖应声弹开，她从粉扑袋里取出粉扑。然后，她从桌上拿起那把玻璃匙，借助它，往那衬白皮的小袋子里，簌簌抖进了一撮她明知是致命毒药的粉末。

她啪的一声合上提包，把盖子放回那只贴着“砒霜”标签的罐子上，拧得紧紧的。随后她从桌边退开几步，一动不动地站着，想着自己方才干下的事。

她为什么要那么做——在她自己嘴里，这是件“古怪”的事？在她心底，她对这无声的疑问其实一清二楚。可她连对自己，也不肯承认这个目的。

===Original===

All at once she heard sounds just behind her﻿—the sounds made by slippered, shuffling feet.

Filled with a sudden shock of sick terror, she turned slowly round to see Roger Gretorex’s old charwoman, Mrs. Huntley, standing uncertainly in the middle of the room.

The woman had evidently let herself in from the back alley with a latchkey. But how long had she been here? And how much had she seen?

“Why, Mrs. Huntley,” said Ivy in a shaken voice. “You did startle me!”

===Chinese===

冷不防，她听见背后响起窸窸窣窣的声音——趿着拖鞋、蹭着地板的脚步声。

一阵翻胃似的惊恐猛地攫住了她。她缓缓转过身，看见罗杰·格雷托雷克斯那位打杂的老女佣亨特利太太（Mrs. Huntley），正迟疑地站在屋子当中。

这女人显然是用弹簧钥匙从后巷自己进来的。可她来了多久了？又瞧见了多少？

“哎呀，亨特利太太，”艾薇声音发颤地说，“你可吓了我一跳！”

===Original===

“Did I, ma’am? I beg your pardon, I’m sure. I just come in to see if the doctor wanted anything,” the old woman spoke in a pleasant, refined voice.

“He’ll be here in a minute﻿—”

“I won’t disturb him now, ma’am. I’ll come in later.”

Opening the door through which she had entered, she slipped through it into the gathering darkness. Then she shut the door noiselessly behind her.

===Chinese===

“是吗，太太？真对不住您。我进来瞧瞧大夫要不要什么东西。”老妇人说话的声音悦耳而有教养。

“他一会儿就过来——”

“这会儿我就不打搅他了，太太。回头我再来。”

她拉开进来的那扇门，一闪身进了渐浓的暮色里，又无声无息地随手把门掩上。

===Original===

Ivy moved again close to the deal table. She felt violently disturbed, even terrified. Yet the old woman had looked absolutely placid, though a little taken aback at finding a lady where she had expected to see nobody, excepting maybe her employer, and him only if he were engaged in making up medicines.

Hardly knowing what she was doing, Ivy Lexton glanced up at the row of books in the shabby little bookcase above the table by which she was standing, and she saw that among them was one entitled, “Poisons.”

She was just about to take it down when she heard quick footsteps in the short passage, and Gretorex opened the surgery door.

“I won’t be more than a few moments now! You will stay, my dearest, won’t you, till I’ve done with this chap?”

===Chinese===

艾薇又挪到松木桌跟前。她心乱如麻，甚至吓得够呛。可那老妇人方才分明是心平气和的，只是料想不到屋里会有一位太太，微微吃了一惊——她原以为除了主人不会见着别人，还须是主人正忙着配药才在。

艾薇·莱克斯顿几乎不知道自己在做什么，抬眼扫了扫她身旁那口寒碜的小书架上一排书——书架就悬在桌子上方——她看见其中一册书名叫《毒物》（Poisons）。

她正要伸手去取，忽听得短过道里响起急促的脚步声，格雷托雷克斯推开了诊室的门。

“这回用不了几分钟了！你等着我，最亲爱的，等我把这家伙打发走，好不好？”

===Original===

“Of course I will. Don’t hurry,” she answered, in a soft, kindly tone.

She took down the book and hurriedly she turned to the index.

Yes! Here was the word she sought. There were a number of references﻿—half a dozen at least﻿—and she turned up, “Effects of Arsenic; page 154.”

A famous case of secret poisoning was quoted, with every detail set out, and she read it with intense, absorbed interest. It told her what she had so much wanted to know, and feared to ask Roger Gretorex, how a secret poisoner went to work, and also how long the process took before﻿—before﻿—even to herself she did not end the question.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

“当然等着你。别急。”她柔声柔气、和蔼可亲地答道。

她取下那本书，急匆匆翻到索引。

找到了！正是她寻的那个词。相关条目有一大串——少说也有五六处——她翻到“砒霜的效用；第154页（page 154）”。

书里引了一件赫赫有名的密投毒案，桩桩细节罗列俱全，她读得如痴如醉、心无旁骛。它告诉了她那件她极想知道、又不敢问罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的事：一个暗中投毒的人是如何着手的，还有，这个过程要多久才——才——连对她自己，这个问题也没有问完。……

===Original===

As she put the volume back on the bookshelf her mind travelled into the future﻿—the now possible, now probable, future.

Standing there, in Gretorex’s barely furnished surgery, she saw herself the cherished wife of Miles Rushworth, and not only rich beyond the dreams of even her desire, but also secure from every conceivable earthly ill.

Had Ivy Lexton belonged to another generation, she would doubtless have called what had just happened to herself “providential.” As it was, she just thought it a piece of astounding, almost incredible, good luck.

She next took down from the shelf a thin little book dealing with infantile paralysis. But she had only just time to open it, and to glance, with a feeling of shrinking distaste, at one of the illustrations, when Gretorex burst into the surgery. “The poor chap’s gone at last! What are you reading, darling?”

===Chinese===

她把书放回书架上，思绪已飘进了未来——那如今既属可能、又近乎必然的未来。

站在那里，站在格雷托雷克斯这间陈设寥寥的诊室里，她看见自己成了迈尔斯·拉什沃思百般珍宠的妻子，不但富贵得连她自己的欲望做梦都不曾梦到，而且从此万般人世的灾祸都伤不着她分毫。

艾薇·莱克斯顿若是生在上一辈人里，无疑会把方才落到自己头上的事称作“天意”。如今她只觉得，这是一桩惊人得近乎不可思议的好运气。

接着她又从架上取下一本讲小儿麻痹症的薄薄小册子。可她才刚翻开，才带着畏缩厌恶之感瞥了一眼其中一幅插图，格雷托雷克斯便一头闯进诊室。“那个可怜的家伙总算走啦！你在读什么呢，亲爱的？”

===Original===

“A book about children, dear.”

There came a pathetic look into her eyes, and Gretorex gently took the thin volume from her hand. Then he kissed that lovely, soft little hand.

She had told him, very early in their acquaintance, that her husband hated the idea of children, as if they had a child it would surely interfere with the kind of idle, gay life that he, Jervis, loved. For the hundredth time Gretorex cursed Lexton for a heartless brute.

She allowed him to take her in his arms. For a few moments they clung together, and she kissed him with real passion, responding as she had not responded for what seemed to Gretorex an eternity of frustrate longing.

===Chinese===

“一本讲孩子的书，亲爱的。”

她眼里浮起一层楚楚可怜的神色，格雷托雷克斯温柔地从她手里接过那本薄册，又吻了吻那只可爱的、软软的小手。

还在他们相识之初，她就告诉过他，说她丈夫一想到孩子就厌恶，因为倘若有了孩子，势必搅了他杰维斯所爱的那种闲散快活的日子。格雷托雷克斯第一百回把莱克斯顿骂作没心没肺的畜生。

她任凭他把自己搂进怀里。两人依偎了片刻，她带着真切的热情吻他，那样地回应着他——在格雷托雷克斯看来，这已是隔了一世之久，隔着他那场永劫般求而不得的漫长渴念。

===Original===

Ivy had been frightened, very much frightened, just now. It was surprisingly comfortable and reassuring to feel his strong arms round her, to know that he loved her﻿—loved her.

“Did you buy an evening paper?” she asked at last, disengaging herself from his close embrace.

“No, but the chap who came to see me had one in his hand, and I believe he left it behind. Would you like me to give it you to read in the omnibus?”

“It would hurt my eyes to do that. I was only wondering what had happened about that Branksome Case?”

===Chinese===

刚才艾薇受了惊吓，吓得不轻。此刻靠在他结实的臂弯里，知道他爱她——爱着她，那份慰藉与安心，竟出乎她自己的意料。

“你买晚报了吗？”她终于问，一面从他紧搂的怀抱里挣出身来。

“没买。不过来找我的那家伙手里拿着一份，我记得他落下了。要不要我给你带上，公共汽车里看？”

“在车上看那个，眼睛要疼的。我不过是想知道，那桩布兰克瑟姆案到底怎么样了？”

===Original===

“I can tell you that without looking at the paper, darling. In fact I did tell you﻿—but you’ve forgotten, my pet.”

“What was it that you told me?”

“That they’ll hang the man, and let the woman off!”

He spoke quite confidently. “There’s not a doubt but that she really planned the whole thing out. But he, poor wretch, bought the fly papers. Most of the secret poisoning that goes on is done by women.”

===Chinese===

“不用看报我也说得出，亲爱的。其实我方才就告诉过你了——可你忘了，我的宝贝。”

“你告诉我什么来着？”

“他们要绞死那个男人，放过那个女人！”

他说得十拿九稳。“毫无疑问，整件事其实都是她谋划的。可是买灭蝇纸的，是他，那个可怜虫。世上来来去去的暗地投毒，多半是女人干的。”

===Original===

“How dreadful!”

“A great many dreadful things go on in this strange world, my darling love.”

“You mean things that are not found out?”

He nodded, almost gaily. He was glad, so glad, to find a subject that interested her, and that might make her stay a few moments longer.

===Chinese===

“多可怕！”

“这个古怪的世界上，可怕的事情多着呢，我的心肝宝贝。”

“你是说，那些没被查出来的事？”

他点了点头，几乎是轻快地点的。他发现了一个让她感兴趣的题目，一个或许能叫她多留几分钟的题目，高兴得很，高兴极了。

===Original===

So, “I’m afraid that only one secret poisoner is found out for six that go scot free,” he went on. “Even now it’s difficult to tell the difference between the effect produced by, say, arsenic, and a very ordinary ailment. A postmortem only takes place if there’s already reason to suspect foul play.”

“Postmortem?”

The word meant nothing to Ivy Lexton.

“In the vast majority of cases the danger is negligible,” he continued. “The secret poisoner, especially if a woman, is never even suspected, and if she is﻿—”

===Chinese===

于是他接着说：“恐怕每六个暗中投毒的人，才有一个被查出来，其余五个都逍遥法外。时至今日，砒霜之类造成的效果，同一桩顶普通的病症，还是难分彼此。除非本来就疑心有人做了手脚，否则验尸（postmortem）这码事根本不会发生。”

“验尸？”

这个词对艾薇·莱克斯顿毫无意义。

“绝大多数情形里，风险微不足道。”他接着说，“暗中投毒的人，尤其要是个女人，压根儿没人疑心；万一疑上了——”

===Original===

“If she is?” echoed Ivy uncertainly.

“The doctor, nine times out of ten, gives her the benefit of the doubt!”

He ended his careless sentence with a laugh. It was of her, of her dear nearness, of her kind, soft, loving manner, that he was thinking, and not at all of what he was saying.

“Would you let her off, Roger?”

===Chinese===

“万一疑上了？”艾薇没把握地跟着问。

“十有八九，医生会宁信其无，放她过去！”

他随口说完这句，笑了起来。他心里想着的是她，是她可亲的近在咫尺，是她那温存柔婉、脉脉含情的模样，压根儿没在想自己嘴里说的是什么。

“换了你，会放过她吗，罗杰？”

===Original===

He grew suddenly grave. “Well, no, I don’t think I would. You see it wouldn’t be right. For one thing, she might try the same game over again.”

She looked at him coldly. Few men were as set on doing right as was apparently this man.

Then she smiled, a curiously subtle, sweet little smile. She knew, if no one else did, the weak place in his defences. It was pleasant to know that no other woman would ever have that knowledge. Ivy believed, rightly or wrongly, that a man does not love twice as this man, Gretorex, loved her.

“Well, I must go now,” she said at last. And then, for she saw the sudden darkening of his face, “You may as well come with me as far as the corner of Great Smith Street, Roger. It’s quite dark now.”

===Chinese===

他忽然正色道：“这个，不会，我想我不会放过。你要明白，那不对。再说了，她保不齐还要故技重施。”

她冷冷地望着他。像这个男人这样一心要做正人君子的，天下少有。

随后她笑了，笑得微妙而甜巧。他防线上的那处薄弱环节，别人不知道，她知道。想到再没有第二个女人能晓得这一层，她心里很受用。艾薇相信——且不论对错——男人的一生里，不会再有第二回像格雷托雷克斯爱她这样的爱法。

“好了，我真得走了。”她终于说。接着，因为看见他的脸倏地阴沉下来，又道：“罗杰，你不妨送我到大史密斯街（Great Smith Street）的街口。天已经全黑了。”

===Original===

Once more she allowed him to kiss her. Once more there came from her the response which had now for so long been lacking, and of which the lack made him feel so desolate.

As they walked along the now shrouded, deserted little back streets of old Westminster, Ivy Lexton was very gentle in her manner to the man who loved her with so wholehearted and selfless a devotion.

She had quite decided that soon they must part, never to meet again. And yet, though Gretorex would never know it, it was to him, albeit indirectly, that she would owe her splendid freedom, and all that freedom was to bring to her. It was that knowledge, maybe, that made her manner so gentle and so kind.

When at last Jervis Lexton came in, he found his wife playing patience in the pretty sitting-room where she spent so little of her time.

===Chinese===

她又一次容他吻她。她身上又一次涌出了那久已缺失的回应——正是这缺失，曾叫他觉得何等孤寂荒凉。

两人走在老威斯敏斯特此刻已被夜色罩住、空无一人的小巷子里，艾薇·莱克斯顿对那个以如此全心全意、不掺杂私的挚爱爱着她的男人，态度温柔极了。

她已经拿定主意，两人不久就得分手，从此不再相见。然而，格雷托雷克斯永远不会知道，她那份锦绣的自由，连同这份自由将要带给她的一切，说到底是亏了他——尽管是间接地亏了他。也许正是这份认知，让她的态度这般温柔、这般和气。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿终于回到家，见妻子正在那间漂亮的客厅里玩单人纸牌——她在那屋里消磨的时日本来少得可怜。

===Original===

He felt a little surprised, for unless she happened to be out, as was the case six nights out of seven, Ivy always went to bed early.

“Thank God, I’m safe home again!” he exclaimed. “It was the most awful show. The grub wasn’t bad, but the champagne was like syrup. I’ve ‘some thirst,’ I can tell you!”

“Wait a minute, and”﻿—she smiled a gay little smile﻿—“I’ll mix you a highball, old boy. Would you like a Bizzy Izzy, just as a treat?”

“The answer, ma’am, is ‘yes’!”

===Chinese===

他略觉诧异，因为艾薇素来睡得早，除非碰巧在外头——一星期倒有六个晚上是这种情形。

“谢天谢地，我可平安到家了！”他嚷道，“那场子糟透了。吃食倒不赖，可那香槟跟糖浆似的。我这会儿‘渴得可以’，不骗你！”

“稍等一会儿，”——她绽开一个快活的小笑——“我给你调杯海波酒（highball），老伙计。想不想来一杯‘比兹·伊兹’（Bizzy Izzy），算你沾个光？”

“回太太的话——‘要’！”

===Original===

She went off into the dining-room, and took from the fine old mahogany brassbound wine-cooler a bottle of rye whisky and a bottle of sherry. Then, carefully, she poured a small wineglass of each into a tall glass.

With the glass in her hand she hurried down the passage, and so into the bright, clean, empty kitchen. There she soon found some ice, and, after having chipped off a number of small pieces, she waited a moment and listened intently, for she did not want to be surprised in what she was about to do.

But the old cook was lying sound asleep in the bedroom which lay beyond the kitchen. Ivy could even hear her long, drawn-out snores.

Opening a cupboard door very, very quietly, she found a syphon, and filled up the glass almost to the top with soda-water. Then, quickly, she mixed in with a clean wooden spoon a good pinch of the powder she had secreted in the pochette of her bag.

===Chinese===

她走进餐室，从那口带黄铜包边的上好老红木酒柜里，取出一瓶黑麦威士忌和一瓶雪莉酒。然后，她小心翼翼地往一只高杯里，各倒了一小杯的量。

她端着杯子，匆匆穿过过道，走进那间明亮、洁净、空无一人的厨房。在那儿她很快寻着了冰，凿下若干小块之后，她静候了片刻，凝神倾听——她可不想在着手要做的事上被人撞见。

可老厨娘正在厨房后头的卧室里睡得死沉。艾薇甚至听得见她那悠长拖沓的鼾声。

她轻之又轻地打开一扇橱柜门，找着一只虹吸苏打水瓶，把杯子灌得几乎齐沿。然后，她动作麻利地拿一把干净的木匙，搅进了满满一撮藏在她提包粉扑袋里的粉末。

===Original===

“A perfect Bizzy Izzy!” Ivy called out gaily as she swiftly went down the corridor, holding in her steady hand the tall glass, now full almost to the brim.

Through the hall and back into the sitting-room she hurried, and then she watched, with an odd sensation of excitement, her husband toss off the delicious iced drink.

“This soda fizz has got a bitter tang to it,” he exclaimed, “but it’s none the worse for that!”

Ivy stayed awake for a long time that night. She had suddenly begun to feel afraid, she hardly knew of what. But at last she dropped off to sleep.

===Chinese===

“一杯地道货的比兹·伊兹！”艾薇喜洋洋地喊道，手上稳稳端着那只几乎满到杯沿的高杯，快步穿过走廊。

她穿过门厅，急急回到客厅，随后带着一种异样的兴奋，看着丈夫把那杯美味沁凉的酒一饮而尽。

“这苏打汽水有点发苦的怪味儿。”他叫道，“不过倒也不碍事！”

那天夜里，艾薇久久没有合眼。她忽然害怕起来，怕什么，她自己也不大明白。但最后她还是沉沉睡去。

===Original===

At nine o’clock the next morning she awoke. What was it that had happened last night? Then she remembered.

Leaping out of bed she rushed across the dressing-table, on which there lay the mother-of-pearl bolster bag she had had out with her last night. Opening it she took out her handkerchief, her powder puff, and her purse. Then she put the bag, now quite empty save for the white powder the tiny white leather-lined inner recess contained, into an old despatch-box which had belonged to her father.

It was the only “lock up” Ivy Lexton possessed; at no time of her life had she been so foolish as to keep dangerous love-letters more than a very short time. She put the despatch-box in what was the empty half of a huge Victorian inlaid wardrobe. Then she got into bed again, and rang the bell.

A moment later the day-maid opened the bedroom door.

“Mr. Lexton was ill in the night, ma’am. He thinks he ate something last evening that didn’t agree with him. He asked me to tell you that he’s not going to the office this morning.”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨九点，她醒了。昨儿夜里出了什么事来着？接着她想起来了。

她一跃下床，奔到梳妆台前——台上搁着昨晚随身带出去的那只珠母镶饰的长枕包。她打开包，取出手帕、粉扑和钱袋。然后她把包收进一只她父亲留下的旧公文箱里，此时包里已空空如也，只剩那衬白皮的小小内袋里盛着的白色粉末。

这是艾薇·莱克斯顿仅有的一件“上锁的家什”；她一生里从没有傻到把危险的情书留过很长时间。她把公文箱塞进那口庞大的维多利亚式嵌木衣柜的空半边，然后重新钻回床上，按铃叫人。

片刻之后，日班女仆推开了卧室的门。

“莱克斯顿先生夜里不舒服，太太。他觉得自己昨晚吃了什么不合适的东西。他让我告诉您，今天早晨不去写字楼了。”

## VI / 六

===Original===
It was the eighth of November, a day which, though she never realised it, altered the whole of Ivy Lexton’s life. And this was the more extraordinary because she was usually quick enough to realise the importance of everything that concerned herself.

But on this day she was feeling secretly excited, anxious, and what to herself she called “nervy,” for her husband’s illness, though it had only lasted just over a week, seemed to her intolerably long-drawn-out.

Jervis Lexton, poor devil, was putting up a grim, instinctive fight for life. Coming of a long line of sporting, out-of-door, country squires and their placid wives, he was magnificently healthy, hard-bitten, and possessed of reserves of physical strength on which he was now drawing daily larger and larger drafts.

On the morning when she had first been told that Jervis had been taken ill in the night, Ivy had gone down to Rushworth’s city office. There, as she put it afterwards when telling the invalid of her interview with Mr. James, the man he called his boss, “red carpets had been put down for her,” and no difficulty at all had been made as to Lexton’s staying away.

===Chinese===
那是十一月八日。这一天，虽然她本人始终未曾察觉，却改变了艾薇·莱克斯顿的一生。而这就愈发显得异乎寻常——因为凡是牵涉到她自己的事，她向来的反应是够快的，什么要紧、什么不要紧，她一概心里有数。

可是这一天，她心里却暗自又是兴奋、又是焦灼——用她私下对自己说的话来讲，是“神经紧张”——因为丈夫的病虽然才算拖了一个多礼拜，在她看来却已经缠绵得叫人难以忍受了。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿这个可怜人，正凭本能打着一场严酷的求生之仗。他出身于绵延数代的乡绅世家——世代是爱好运动、惯于户外生活的乡村士绅，配上各自娴静的妻子——生得一副极壮健的体格，吃苦耐劳，身子骨里蓄着雄厚的本钱，而眼下，他正一天比一天更大笔地从这本钱里支取。

头一回听说杰维斯夜里病倒的那个上午，艾薇便赶到了拉什沃思在城里的办公处。事后她向病人讲起自己同詹姆斯先生（Mr. James）——就是杰维斯称作“老板”的那位——会面的情形时说，“人家给她铺上了红地毯”，而且对莱克斯顿告假不上班这件事，半点为难的意思都没有。

===Original===
As a matter of fact, the young man had very soon been sized up as being, from a business point of view, hopeless. But his pleasant, easy manners, and his inexhaustible fund of small talk and of good stories, amused the boss. Also﻿—and that, naturally, was the one thing that mattered﻿—Jervis Lexton was a pet of Miles Rushworth.

After Mrs. Jervis Lexton’s visit to the office, however, what had seemed a mystery had been at any rate partially explained. What man, so Rushworth’s London agent asked himself smiling, could resist that deliciously pretty and sweet-mannered little woman? No wonder a job had been invented for her husband, who was, after all, a decent chap.

A day was to come when Mr. James would try to remember how Ivy Lexton had impressed him, and when all he would succeed in remembering, very vividly, was how agreeable that impression had been, and how touchingly the lovely lady had revealed her devotion to her husband, fortunate Jervis Lexton.

On the second day Jervis had said he felt so queer that he would like to see their old friend, Dr. Lancaster. And by now, after five days, that genial general practitioner, though utterly unsuspicious of the truth, was nevertheless becoming slightly uneasy at the persistence of the illness.

===Chinese===
其实，人们很快便掂量出，从生意眼光看，这位年轻人是无药可救的。但他那讨人喜欢的随和气度，他那永远讲不完的闲谈趣话，倒叫老板觉得有趣。何况——这自然才是顶要紧的一条——杰维斯·莱克斯顿是迈尔斯·拉什沃思的宠儿。

不过，等杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太来办公处走过一遭之后，那桩原本看似蹊跷的事，至少也弄明白了一半。拉什沃思的伦敦代理笑吟吟地暗自问道：这样一位娇俏可人、仪态温存的小妇人，哪个男人招架得住？怪不得要为她丈夫凭空造出一个差事来——毕竟他也是个正派人。

将来会有那么一天，詹姆斯先生会努力回想艾薇·莱克斯顿当初给他留下了怎样的印象；而他所能忆起的——想得真真切切——只是那份印象何等令人愉快，还有那位美丽的太太流露对丈夫的挚爱时，又是何等动人；她的丈夫，便是有福气的杰维斯·莱克斯顿。

第二天，杰维斯便说自己难受得厉害，想见见他们的老朋友兰开斯特医生（Dr. Lancaster）。到如今已是第五天，这位和气的全科医生虽然对真相毫无疑心，却也渐渐为病势的缠绵感到隐隐不安了。

===Original===
He had insisted, much against Ivy’s will, on sending in a nurse, a placid, kindly woman named Bradfield, who had often nursed for the doctor before.

Small wonder that the patient’s wife was also becoming just a little fretful, and more than a little anxious. How long, she often asked herself restlessly, was her ordeal going to last?

Yet another fact added to Ivy Lexton’s discomfort during those long days of waiting.

That fact, or rather problem, concerned Roger Gretorex. She found it increasingly difficult to prevent him from coming to the flat. When alone with her he made no secret of his dislike of meeting her husband on “Hail fellow, well met!” terms, and yet he longed to be with her every moment of his scanty leisure.

===Chinese===
他不顾艾薇十分不愿意，坚持派来一名护士——一位性情平和、心地善良的女人，姓布拉德菲尔德（Bradfield），从前常在兰开斯特医生手下看护病人。

无怪乎病人的妻子也渐渐变得有些烦躁，而且不止有些焦虑了。她时常心神不定地暗自盘问：她这场劫难，究竟还要熬多久？

在那漫长等待的日子里，还有一桩事，更添了艾薇·莱克斯顿的心烦意乱。

这桩事，或者不如说这个难题，关乎罗杰·格雷托雷克斯。她越来越拦不住他不上公寓来。两人单独相处时，他毫不掩饰自己的厌恶——不愿同她丈夫做那种“见面熟稔、称兄道弟”的应酬——可他仅有的那一点闲暇，却又渴望分分秒秒都同她在一起。

===Original===
At times she felt she almost hated him, for by now her whole mind was filled with the thought of Rushworth, and of all that she felt convinced Rushworth was going to mean in her life. But she could not yet afford to break with Gretorex. Afford, indeed, was still the right word, for again he was supplying her with what had always been to Ivy the staff of life﻿—petty cash.

But she came to one great resolution, and that was to go no more to the humble little house in the Westminster slum with which she had now a secret, terrifying association. And so, as her slightest wish was law to Gretorex, the two began meeting now in a picture gallery, or, when it was a fine day, in Kensington Gardens, which was conveniently near the charming flat the Lextons owed to the generous kindness of Miles Rushworth.

So far Ivy had managed to conceal her husband’s illness from Gretorex. With regard to that mysterious illness, it was of her lover, and of her lover alone, that up to now she had felt afraid.

As a matter of fact Roger Gretorex had already completely forgotten that idle talk of theirs concerning the Branksome poisoning mystery. But every word that had been uttered during the evening when she had had supper at Ferry Place, and every moment that she had spent in the surgery, remained uncannily present to Ivy’s mind.

===Chinese===
有时她觉得自己简直恨他了：因为这时她满心满脑只有拉什沃思，只有她笃信拉什沃思将在她生命里成就的一切。然而她眼下还“赔不起”同格雷托雷克斯翻脸的代价——“赔得起赔不起”，这字眼在这儿倒是用得再准不过，因为又是他在供给她一样东西，一样向来被艾薇当作活命之本的东西——零用钱。

但她也下定决心做了桩大决断：再也不去威斯敏斯特贫民区里那所寒酸小宅了——那地方如今在她心里，已蒙上一层秘密而可怖的联想。于是，既然她一颦一笑在格雷托雷克斯那里便如圣旨，两人便改在一座画廊里会面；遇上晴天，便到肯辛顿花园去——花园离莱克斯顿夫妇那套可爱的公寓近便得很，而这公寓，还得感念迈尔斯·拉什沃思的慷慨情分。

直到如今，艾薇总算把丈夫生病的事瞒住了格雷托雷克斯。至于那场蹊跷的病，她一直心怀惧怕的，是她的情人——而且单单只是她的情人。

其实，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯早把他们那晚关于布兰克索姆投毒疑案（Branksome poisoning mystery）的闲谈忘得干干净净了。可是她在费里街吃晚饭那晚上说出口的每一句话、她在那间诊室里度过的每一刻，却鬼使神差地时时浮现在艾薇心头。

---

===Original===
And now, on this eighth of November, she had been out for an hour, looking into the shop windows which line Kensington High Street. She had even gone into one famous emporium and bought a new, and very expensive, black model hat. Though quite unobtrusive in shape, it was at once as simple and as unusual as only a French model hat can be. She had felt that she might have cause to be really very much put out if that perfect little hat were bought, over her head so to speak, during the next few days.

At last, feeling more cheerful, she walked briskly back to the Duke of Kent Mansion. The pleasant-spoken porter﻿—all men, even lift porters, were always pleasant-spoken to Ivy Lexton﻿—took her up in the lift. She let herself in with her latchkey, for she always liked slipping in and out of the flat alone.

As she went through the hall towards her charming bedroom, the door of her husband’s room opened, and Nurse Bradfield came out, looking flustered and worried.

“Mr. Lexton doesn’t seem so well, and as we haven’t seen Dr. Lancaster for two days, I telephoned to say I’d like him to come now. But oh, Mrs. Lexton, such a dreadful thing has happened!”

===Chinese===
而到了这十一月八日，她出门逛了一个钟头，一家家看肯辛顿大街（Kensington High Street）两旁的橱窗。她甚至走进一家有名的大商场，买了一顶崭新的、价格不菲的黑色时装原版帽（model hat）。帽形虽然毫不惹眼，却既透着简洁、又透着别致，唯有法国时装帽才有这等本事。她心里已觉出：倘若这顶十全十美的小帽子，在这几天里被人照俗话说“当面”抢了先买了去，她可真要大为懊恼了。

终于，她觉得心头松快了些，便迈开轻快的步子走回肯特公爵大厦。那位说话客气的门房——天下的男人，连开电梯的侍者也不例外，对艾薇·莱克斯顿说话从来都是和和气气的——把她送上电梯。她自己用弹簧锁钥匙开了门进去，因为她一向喜欢独自一人悄悄进出公寓。

她穿过门厅、朝自己那间雅致的卧室走去时，丈夫房间的门开了，布拉德菲尔德护士走了出来，一脸慌张忧急的神色。

“莱克斯顿先生看上去不大好，我们又有两天没见着兰开斯特医生了，我就打了电话，说我希望他现在就来。可是哎呀，莱克斯顿太太，出了件糟透的事！”

===Original===
Under her delicately applied rouge, the colour drifted from Ivy’s face.

“Something dreadful?” she repeated mechanically.

“Yes, indeed, for Dr. Lancaster has gone and broken his leg playing golf! He was staying with his brother-in-law at Seaford, for one night only, but now he’s laid up there, and they don’t know for how long.”

Ivy’s first feeling was one of relief. She had been so dreadfully frightened just now lest “something” should have been found out.

But even so, as the nurse went on speaking, there did come over her a slight feeling of misgiving.

===Chinese===
脂粉虽敷得清淡，血色还是从艾薇脸上褪了下去。

“糟透的事？”她机械地重复了一遍。

“可不是嘛！兰开斯特医生打高尔夫球，竟把腿摔断了！他原是到西福德（Seaford）他妹夫家做客，只住一夜的，如今却躺倒在那儿了，还不知道要躺多久呢。”

艾薇头一个感觉是松了口气。方才她是吓坏了，生怕“什么事”被发现了。

但即便如此，护士往下说的时候，她心头还是掠过了一丝疑惧。

===Original===
“However, Dr. Berwick, the doctor who works in with Dr. Lancaster, so to speak, is coming round instead!” Nurse Bradfield continued.

“I hope he’s nice,” said Ivy earnestly.

The accident to Dr. Lancaster was real bad luck. He was a dear old thing, and so truly fond of her. When they had been almost penniless, he had attended her twice for nothing.

There came a curious look over the nurse’s face. She hesitated, and then made up her mind to be frank.

“To tell you the truth, Mrs. Lexton, I don’t much like Dr. Berwick! But then I’m prejudiced, for I once had a nasty little scrap with him when I was nursing another case for Dr. Lancaster. I consider that Dr. Berwick was very rude to me.”

===Chinese===
“不过，接替出诊的是伯威克医生（Dr. Berwick）——可以说，是同兰开斯特医生搭伙行医的那位！”布拉德菲尔德护士接着说。

“但愿他是个好人。”艾薇恳切地说。

兰开斯特医生遭此意外，真是倒霉。他是位可亲的老先生，又真心疼她。当年他们夫妇几乎身无分文的时候，他两次给她看病，分文不取。

护士脸上掠过一丝异样的神情。她迟疑了一下，随即打定主意，索性说了实话。

“跟您说句实话吧，莱克斯顿太太，我不大喜欢那位伯威克医生！不过我心存偏见，因为我从前替兰开斯特医生看护另一个病人时，同他小小地龃龉过一场。依我看，伯威克医生对我很是无礼。”

===Original===
“How horrid of him!” exclaimed Ivy sympathetically, although she had not really been attending to what Nurse Bradfield was saying.

But she did listen, with startled attention, when the nurse suddenly added:

“However, he’s said to be very clever, and he’s much more up-to-date than Dr. Lancaster. I shouldn’t be a bit surprised if Dr. Berwick finds out what really is the matter with Mr. Lexton!”

Ivy stared fearfully at the speaker, and again there swept over her a strong feeling of misgiving, if not of fear.

===Chinese===
“他可真不像话！”艾薇同情地嚷道，其实布拉德菲尔德护士方才说的话，她根本没怎么听进去。

可护士突然又添上一句时，她倒是惊惶地凝神听了：

“不过，据说他非常高明，也比兰开斯特医生紧跟新学理。要是伯威克医生查出莱克斯顿先生到底得的是什么病，我一点儿也不奇怪！”

艾薇惊恐地瞪着说话的人，一阵强烈的疑惧——若非恐惧——再度席卷了她全身。

---

===Original===
Thus was effected the entrance of Dr. Berwick into the lives of Ivy Lexton and her husband.

In spite of his shrewdness and long professional experience, it took the new doctor some days to become even vaguely puzzled﻿—so true is it that murder is the one chink in the armour of a civilised community﻿—over certain unusual features in his patient’s case.

On the occasion of his first visit to Jervis Lexton, Dr. Berwick had been in a great hurry, and though he had seen his new patient’s wife for a few moments, he had impatiently dismissed her from his mind as a foolish, frivolous little woman. Indeed, considering the manner of man he was, he could hardly have told, after that brief interview in the dimly lighted hall, whether young Mrs. Lexton was pretty, or just ordinary, though he had noticed, with disapproval, that she was very much made up.

Ivy on her side, if feeling slightly surprised by the doctor’s lack of interest in her attractive self, was at the same time reassured by the fact that Dr. Berwick evidently thought her husband only suffering from a temporary, if obstinate ailment, brought on, most probably, by something he had eaten during the evening which had preceded the night he had first been taken ill.

===Chinese===
伯威克医生就这样走进了艾薇·莱克斯顿和她丈夫的生活。

这位新来的医生尽管精明、尽管行医多年阅历深厚，也过了好些天，才对病人症候中几处不寻常的疑点隐约生疑——文明社会的甲胄上，凶杀正是那唯一的一道裂隙，此话一点不假。

头一回上门看杰维斯·莱克斯顿那次，伯威克医生赶时间，虽然同这位新病人的妻子照面了几分钟，却不耐烦地把她在心里一笔勾销，当成个愚蠢轻佻的小妇人。说实在的，以他那样一个人，在那光线幽暗的门厅里匆匆一面之后，他实在说不上年轻的莱克斯顿太太究竟是漂亮还是寻常，虽然他带着非难之意注意到了：她妆化得很浓。

在艾薇这一方面呢，医生对她这妩媚人儿竟无动于衷，她虽略感意外，但伯威克医生显然认为她丈夫害的不过是种一时的小恙——只是缠手罢了，多半是初次发病前那天晚上吃了什么东西吃出来的——这一点又让她放了心。

===Original===
And yet her feeling of misgiving prevailed so far that she deliberately tried to keep out of the new doctor’s way. This was quite easy, for she was out to most meals, and owing to Lexton’s illness she had stopped giving her pleasant little bridge-parties at the flat.

But as the days dragged on, as Jervis Lexton, instead of responding to treatment, grew steadily worse, Dr. Berwick began to feel really puzzled. He made up his mind one day to see Mrs. Lexton. On that day Ivy was going to be motored to Brighton by a new admirer, and she had said she would come in after lunch to fetch her fur coat. So the doctor, believing she would be back soon, waited for her. But the moments became minutes, and the minutes mounted up to close on half an hour.

Feeling very much annoyed, he was just about to leave the flat, when Ivy walked into the drawing-room, looking, as he instantly acknowledged to himself, charmingly pretty and gay.

“I waited to see you, Mrs. Lexton, because I am not satisfied with your husband’s condition. From what the nurse tells me, Dr. Lancaster was puzzled too, though he said nothing of that in the notes he sent me concerning the case.”

===Chinese===
话虽如此，那股疑惧心理还是占了上风，她便存心躲着这位新医生。这倒不难做到，因为一日三餐她多半在外头吃；又因为莱克斯顿病着，公寓里那些赏心悦目的小小桥牌会，她也停办了。

可日子一天天拖过去，杰维斯·莱克斯顿非但不见起色，反倒一天沉似一天，伯威克医生当真犯起了嘀咕。有一天，他决意要见莱克斯顿太太。那天艾薇正要由一位新拜倒在她裙下的倾慕者开车带去布莱顿（Brighton），她说过午饭后要回来取皮大衣。于是医生料着她快回来了，便等她。可是一瞬拖成了一分，一分又积成了将近半个钟头。

他正自恼火，眼看就要拂袖离开公寓，艾薇偏偏这时走进了客厅——他当即在心里承认，她看上去娇媚动人，一脸欢快。

“莱克斯顿太太，我在这儿等你，是因为你丈夫的病情不能让我满意。听护士的话音，兰开斯特医生也曾纳闷，只是他寄给我的病案记录里，对此只字未提。”

===Original===
Then, almost in spite of himself, he was touched by the look of distress which at once shadowed her lovely face, and it was in a kinder tone that he went on:

“If he does not pick up in the next day or two, I should very much like to have another opinion. Will you try to persuade him to see a specialist?”

“Of course I will,” she said quickly. “Though he can’t bear any fuss made over him, poor old boy! He very much objected to our having a nurse; but she’s such a comfort to me.”

Dr. Berwick disliked Nurse Bradfield. He thought her slow and old-fashioned. So now he told himself that, though she might be a comfort to Mrs. Lexton, he would far prefer a different kind of nurse to tend one as ill as he now realised Jervis Lexton to be.

===Chinese===
随即，几乎是不由自主地，他被她可爱的脸上顿时蒙上的那层忧色打动了，便用温和了些的口吻接着说：

“要是他这一两天还不见起色，我很想再请人看一次。你能不能劝他见见专科医生？”

“我当然劝。”她马上答道，“虽说给他多操心他就受不了，可怜的人！家里添个护士，他还老大不情愿呢；可她真是我的一个大安慰。”

伯威克医生不喜欢布拉德菲尔德护士，嫌她迟缓、老派。于是他此刻暗自盘算：她纵然是莱克斯顿太太的安慰，可他如今已明白杰维斯·莱克斯顿病得有多重，护理这样重的病人，他宁可换一位另一路子的护士。

===Original===
He looked fixedly at his patient’s wife, debating within himself whether he ought to impart to her a suspicion which was beginning, only beginning, to touch his mind. Now and again, during the last two days, he had felt a slight, half-doubting suspicion as to whether certain untoward symptoms could possibly mean that Lexton was absorbing some form of irritant poison. But even to hint or half-imply such possibility is a very serious thing to do from a doctor’s point of view, and so he fell back on what seemed the wiser course of recommending a second opinion.

“Do you think Dr. Lancaster will be away long?” asked Ivy. “He’s such an old friend of mine,” and she smiled, a pretty, disarming little smile. “I know you’re ever so much more clever than he is﻿—”

Dr. Berwick interrupted harshly, “That’s quite untrue! Dr. Lancaster has had ten times the experience I’ve had, and I take my hat off to him every time. But I fear he’s not likely to be back for a long time. You see, he’s no longer a young man, and it is a great chance for him to be invalided away from home, and where he can’t be got at by the kind attentions of friends and﻿—patients.”

===Chinese===
他定定地望着病人的妻子，心里掂量着该不该向她透露一桩刚在心里冒头——才刚刚冒头——的疑心。近两天来，他不时隐约生出一点半信半疑的怀疑：某些险恶的症候，会不会意味着莱克斯顿正吸收着某种刺激性的毒物。可是从医生的角度看，哪怕只是暗示一下、只是半含半露地点出这种可能性，也是非同小可的事，于是他退而求其次，采取了看来更稳妥的办法——建议再请一位医生会诊。

“你看兰开斯特医生要离开很久吗？”艾薇问，“他是我多年的老朋友了。”她嫣然一笑，一个娇俏的、令人解除戒心的小小微笑。“我知道你比他高明得多——”

伯威克医生生硬地打断她：“这话全然不对！兰开斯特医生的经验十倍于我，我对他佩服得五体投地。不过恐怕他一时半会儿回不来。你要知道，他已不年轻了，这一病倒在外头、离开了家门，倒是天赐的休养良机，朋友们那些好心的殷勤——还有病家——也都够不着他了。”

---

===Original===
The next day an untoward thing happened which, though it seemed at the time of very little account, was yet to prove of considerable moment.

Roger Gretorex ran across a Mrs. Horley, whom he had often met in Ivy’s company in the now faraway days when he would go anywhere, and everywhere, just to see her, and to hold her hand for a moment. Mrs. Horley naturally alluded to Jervis Lexton’s illness, and was evidently much surprised to find that it was all news to him. To everyone but Gretorex, Ivy constantly mentioned her husband’s unfortunate condition, and expressed some measure of anxiety.

Though only half believing Mrs. Horley’s tale, or rather believing that Lexton was suffering from some slight indisposition, Gretorex went off to the flat, only to find that Ivy was out, as usual.

The cook had opened the front door. She knew Gretorex quite well by sight, for in the early days of the Lextons being there, he had been an occasional visitor.

===Chinese===
第二天，出了一桩岔子。事情当时看似无关紧要，日后却要显得非同小可。

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯撞见了霍利太太（Mrs. Horley）。在那如今已觉遥远的岁月里，他为了见艾薇一面、握一握她的手，天涯海角都肯去，那时节他常在艾薇身边遇见这位太太。霍利太太自然说起杰维斯·莱克斯顿的病，发觉他竟全然不知，显然十分诧异。除了对格雷托雷克斯，艾薇可是常常把丈夫的不幸病况挂在嘴边，还要发一番焦虑之词的。

霍利太太的话他只信了一半——或者说，他只当莱克斯顿害了点无关紧要的小病——格雷托雷克斯还是赶到了公寓，不料艾薇又照例出门去了。

开门的是厨娘。她同格雷托雷克斯很有些面熟，因为莱克斯顿夫妇刚搬来那阵子，他偶尔也来走动。

===Original===
He listened to her wordy account of “the master’s” illness; and then the nurse, hearing voices in the hall, opened her patient’s door.

“Nurse! This is Dr. Gretorex, a great friend of the master and missus,” said cook, retreating down the corridor towards her kitchen.

Nurse Bradfield, who found life very dull just now, was pleased to see the fine-looking young man﻿—a doctor, too.

“Will you ask Mr. Lexton if he would care to see me?” asked Gretorex.

He felt he could do nothing less, as he was there, and Ivy was out.

===Chinese===
他听她絮絮叨叨地讲“老爷”的病情；这时护士听得门厅里有人说话，便打开了病人的房门。

“护士太太！这位是格雷托雷克斯医生，老爷太太的好朋友。”厨娘说着，退身顺着走廊回厨房去了。

布拉德菲尔德护士这些日子正觉着日子闷得慌，见了这位仪表堂堂的年轻人——还是位医生——很是高兴。

“能不能请问一下莱克斯顿先生，愿不愿意见我？”格雷托雷克斯问。

他觉得人到跟前了，艾薇又不在家，这一点情面总不好不顾。

===Original===
Poor Jervis eagerly, even joyfully, welcomed the suggestion, and the nurse left the two young men together. They talked of all kinds of things﻿—things that interested Lexton rather than Gretorex.

At last the visitor rose. “I’ll be going now﻿—”

“Must you? I get so bored lying here. I wish, old chap, you could think of something that would make me feel the thing again? I don’t think much of Ivy’s Dr. Lancaster. Besides, he’s just broken his leg!”

Then he went into details of what had become his wretched case, and, after having heard him out, Gretorex produced a paper pad and a fountain pen. Rapidly he began writing out a prescription, and he was so absorbed in what he was doing that he did not hear the bedroom door open.

===Chinese===
可怜的杰维斯急切地——简直是欢天喜地地——欢迎这个提议，护士便让两个年轻人单独待在一处。他们天南海北地聊——聊的都是莱克斯顿感兴趣的话题，倒不是格雷托雷克斯的。

最后客人站起身来。“我这就走了——”

“非走不可吗？躺在这儿闷死我了。老兄，你要是能想出个什么方子，叫我觉得身上又对劲儿了才好！艾薇那位兰开斯特医生，我不怎么看好。再说，他这不刚摔断了腿嘛！”

接着他把自己这桩苦不堪言的病，细细说了一遍。听完之后，格雷托雷克斯掏出一本处方笺和一支自来水笔，飞快地开起方子来。他全神贯注在手底的事上，竟没听见卧室的门开了。

===Original===
Dr. Berwick, who was tired, having been up all night over an anxious case, stared with anger, as well as amazement, at his as yet unknown colleague.

“Hallo!” cried Jervis, trying to lift himself up from the pillow, for he had become very weak. “Here’s a lark! An impromptu consultation, eh?”

Then, as even he realised that Dr. Berwick looked like a thundercloud, as he afterwards expressed it to the nurse, he went on, apologetically: “I was joking of course! Gretorex, this is Dr. Berwick, who has taken over Dr. Lancaster’s patients.”

Then, looking at Berwick, he went on: “Dr. Gretorex is a great friend of mine and Ivy’s. He came in just now, and I told him about my poor dry throat and asked him if he could think of something that might give me a little relief. You don’t mind, do you?”

Dr. Berwick waited a moment. Then he said, in far from a pleasant tone:

===Chinese===
伯威克医生守着一个叫人悬心的病人熬了一整夜，正疲惫不堪，此刻又惊又怒，瞪着这位他还不认识的同行。

“喂！”杰维斯喊道，一面竭力想从枕头上撑起身子——他已经虚弱得很了。“妙啊！这算是来了一场即兴会诊，是吧？”

随后，连他也觉出伯威克医生的脸色阴沉得像雷雨将至——他后来是这么向护士形容的——便赔着不是接着说：“我当然是开玩笑！格雷托雷克斯，这位是伯威克医生，接了兰开斯特医生的那班病人。”

接着他又望着伯威克说：“格雷托雷克斯医生是我和艾薇的好朋友。他刚进来，我跟他说了我这可怜的嗓子干得难受，问他能不能想点什么法子，让我稍微松快一点。您不介意吧？”

伯威克医生沉吟了片刻，然后开口，语气绝不客气：

===Original===
“Well, to tell you the truth, I do mind. You are my patient, Mr. Lexton, not Dr. Gretorex’s patient.”

Gretorex rose from the chair on which he had been sitting close to the sick man’s bed. The colour rushed up all over his dark face. He said stiffly, cursing Lexton for a fool the while:

“Mr. Lexton had just told me that Dr. Lancaster has broken his leg. I had no idea that someone else had already taken over the case﻿—”

There followed an awkward silence between the two men. Dr. Berwick was waiting for the formal apology which the other did not consider it necessary to tender to him.

===Chinese===
“这么说吧，跟您交个底：我是介意的。您是我的病人，莱克斯顿先生，不是格雷托雷克斯医生的病人。”

格雷托雷克斯从紧挨病床的那把椅子上站起身。血色涌上他黝黑的脸。他板着面孔说话，一面在心里把莱克斯顿骂作蠢货：

“莱克斯顿先生刚才告诉我，兰开斯特医生摔断了腿。我并不知道这病已经另有医生接手——”

两个男人之间继而起了一阵尴尬的沉默。伯威克医生等着对方正式道歉，而那位却觉得大可不必。

===Original===
At last Gretorex took the piece of paper on which he had written out a prescription for a soothing mixture and tore it in two.

“Well, Lexton,” he exclaimed, “I’ll be off now, leaving you, I’m sure, in excellent hands! Tell Mrs. Lexton I came in. I’ve been so awfully busy this last fortnight that I haven’t had a minute to myself. I’ve taken over for a friend a practice in Westminster for a bit. It’s in a slum, and means a lot of work﻿—”

“And precious little pay, eh?” said Lexton.

Roger Gretorex smiled grimly, “But it’s all experience.”

Then he went out of the room, with just a cool nod to the other doctor.

===Chinese===
最后，格雷托雷克斯拿起那张写着一剂安神合剂处方的纸，撕作两半。

“好了，莱克斯顿，”他扬声道，“我这就走，你算是在顶稳妥的人手里了，这点我信得过！替我告诉莱克斯顿太太一声，我来过。这两个礼拜我忙得不可开交，一分钟都没归过自己。我替一位朋友代管一处诊所，在威斯敏斯特，得管上一阵子。地方在贫民区，活儿多得吓人——”

“进项也少得可怜，是吧？”莱克斯顿说。

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯冷冷一笑：“可这都是历练。”

说完他便出了房间，只朝另一位医生冷淡地点了点头。

---

===Original===
It was a very different Dr. Berwick who at eight that same evening finished eating his well-cooked, daintily-served dinner. Janey Berwick was what her husband’s old friends called, with truth, a thoroughly nice woman. After their early marriage she and her husband had had a bitter struggle, but that had only made them come the closer to one another, and now that he was beginning to be really successful, she was determined to make him, so far as lay in her power, comfortable.

She wore tonight her prettiest evening frock, and anyone, seeing them sitting there side by side in front of their cheerful fire in their pleasant sitting-room, would have thought them a pair of engaged lovers, rather than a couple who had been wedded for close on twelve years.

Janey Berwick still looked a young woman, for she had been only nineteen when she had given up a comfortable, even luxurious, home, to throw in her lot with the young man who till two or three months before their wedding had been still a medical student.

He bore more signs than did his wife of the struggle they had gone through. However, that struggle was now a thing of the past, or, at least, so they both had good reason to think.

===Chinese===
当晚八点钟，吃完那顿烹调得法、摆设精致的晚餐的，便是另一副样子的伯威克医生了。杰妮·伯威克（Janey Berwick）——她丈夫的老朋友们这样评她，且评得名副其实——是位彻头彻尾的好女人。他们早婚之后，夫妻俩曾有过一段苦苦挣扎的岁月，但那只会叫两人贴得更紧；如今他事业上眼见真要出头了，她便打定主意，要尽自己之力让他过得舒坦。

今晚她穿上了她最漂亮的一件晚礼服。谁要是瞧见他们并肩坐在那间舒适的起居室里、守着一炉欢腾的炉火，准会当他们是订了婚的一对恋人，而想不到是结缡将近十二年的夫妻。

杰妮·伯威克看上去仍像个少妇，因为当年她舍下一个舒适——甚至称得上奢侈——的家，把自己的命运同一个年轻人拴在一起时，才只有十九岁；而那年轻人直到婚礼前两三个月，还是个医学院的学生。

那一场挣扎，他身上留下的印记比妻子多。不过，这场挣扎如今已成往事——至少，他们俩都有充分的理由这样认为。

===Original===
An intelligent doctor either shares everything or nothing with his wife. Berwick shared everything and now he was engaged in telling her about Jervis Lexton, and how puzzled he was fast becoming over Lexton’s curious condition. He also told her how surprised, not to say indignant, he had been to discover, when he had gone to see his patient today, another doctor there, actually prescribing for Lexton﻿—true, only as a friend, but acting, even so, in a most irregular fashion!

“I think I made him feel what I thought of such conduct,” he said with satisfaction.

Then suddenly he asked her a question.

“D’you remember that your people took a shooting in Sussex many years ago, when I first knew you, from a man call Gretorex?”

===Chinese===
一个精明的医生，对妻子要么无话不说，要么只字不提。伯威克是无话不说的那一种，此刻他正对她讲杰维斯·莱克斯顿的事，讲自己怎样对莱克斯顿古怪的病况越来越犯疑。他还讲了今天上门看病人时，发现那儿竟另有一位医生、居然在给莱克斯顿开方子，他又是怎样地惊奇——甚至可以说是愤慨——虽说那人只是以朋友身份行事，可这么办事也实在不合规矩！

“我想，我让他明白了我对这种行径的看法。”他心满意足地说。

接着，他突然问了她一个问题。

“你还记得吗？许多年前，我刚认识你的时候，你家在萨塞克斯租下一处猎场，是从一个姓格雷托雷克斯的人手里租的？”

===Original===
“Of course I do, darling! Anchorford was the name of the place.”

She was puzzled at the sudden change in the conversation.

“Well, this young chap I found prescribing for Lexton is Roger Gretorex! I had a sort of feeling I’d seen him before.”

“But what an extraordinary thing﻿—I mean, that he should be a doctor.”

“I don’t see why. Only the old house and that bit of shooting belonged to them, even then. D’you remember Mrs. Gretorex? She was very much the grande dame﻿—”

===Chinese===
“当然记得，亲爱的！那地方叫安克福德（Anchorford）。”

话题陡然一转，她不免纳闷。

“嗨，今天我撞见给莱克斯顿开方子的那个小伙子，正是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯！我就觉得眼熟嘛。”

“可这有多稀奇——我是说，他竟当上了医生。”

“我看这有什么稀奇。那时候，他们家也只剩下一所老宅子和那一小片猎场了。你还记得格雷托雷克斯太太吗？她可是十足的贵妇人（grande dame）派头——”

===Original===
“Yes, she was in a way, but so really kind. She took a great fancy to me,” said Janey Berwick slowly.

“I don’t think she approved of me. She thought you ought to do better.”

Berwick’s wife smiled. It was true that Mrs. Gretorex hadn’t much cared for the dour, silent, medical student who was obviously in love with her attractive young friend.

“It all comes back to me. They were fearfully poor; but Mrs. Gretorex was keeping up all her charities in the village just as if she had still been the rich lady of the manor. I thought it splendid of her. What is Roger Gretorex like now? He was such a handsome boy,” she concluded, with some curiosity.

Her husband waited a moment, then he answered: “He’s still good-looking; I can tell you that much. But I didn’t like the look of him. He said that he’d taken over a slum practice, somewhere in Westminster.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，她派头是足，可心眼儿实在好。她顶喜欢我。”杰妮·伯威克慢悠悠地说。

“我看她不大瞧得上我。她觉得你该攀门更好的亲。”

伯威克的妻子微微一笑。诚然，格雷托雷克斯太太当年对那个面容冷峻、沉默寡言的医学生没有多少好感——他分明是爱上了她那位讨人喜欢的年轻朋友。

“我全想起来了。他们穷得厉害；可格雷托雷克斯太太照旧在村子里维持着她所有的慈善事，俨然还是那位富有的庄园女主人。我那时真佩服她。罗杰·格雷托雷克斯如今长成什么样了？他小时候可是个俊俏少年。”她带着几分好奇，这么收了尾。

她丈夫顿了一顿，才答道：“他如今还是仪表堂堂——这一点我尽可奉告。可我不喜欢他那副神气。他说他接手了一处贫民区诊所，就在威斯敏斯特什么地方。”

===Original===
“Is he a great friend of Mr. Lexton?”

“He seemed to be, though they’re as different as chalk from cheese. The one’s a born idler, the other I should say a born worker; though, mind you, Squire Gretorex was a bad man. D’you remember the sort of things we were told about him, Janey? How he had come in for thirty thousand a year when he was twenty-one, and how by the time he was fifty he had run through the whole of his fortune on the turf?”

“I expect Dr. Gretorex takes after his mother,” she said with a smile.

Suddenly there came the postman’s loud knock, and Berwick, jumping up, went out into the hall.

He came back with only one rather bulky letter, addressed to himself, in a woman’s sloping handwriting as yet unknown to him.

===Chinese===
“他是莱克斯顿先生的好朋友吗？”

“看样子是，虽说他们俩天差地远。一个天生是懒汉，另一个，依我看，天生是干活的命。不过你听着，格雷托雷克斯老乡绅（Squire Gretorex）可不是个好东西。人家讲他的那些事，你还记得吗，杰妮？说他二十一岁上继承了一年三万镑的进项，到五十岁上，家当已经叫他在赛马上挥霍得一干二净？”

“我料想格雷托雷克斯医生随他母亲。”她含笑说。

忽然传来邮差重重的敲门声，伯威克一跃而起，出门到门厅去了。

他手里只拿回来一封厚厚的信，是写给他的，出自一个女人笔迹斜长的手，那笔迹他还陌生。

===Original===
He opened the large, square, pale-mauve envelope slowly, deliberately. It contained a note folded in two, and also an enclosure, an envelope on which was written “Prescriptions.”

He glanced over the note:

> Dear Dr. Berwick,
> You asked me to send you Dr. Lancaster’s prescriptions. I found them just after you left. Jervis is feeling better this afternoon, and the nurse says that if you’re busy she doesn’t think you need come tomorrow.
> Yours sincerely,
> Ivy Lexton.

He looked across at his wife. “It’s from Mrs. Lexton. She says her husband’s better, and that I need not go there tomorrow. That’s a comfort!”

Idly he took out what that other envelope contained. Dr. Lancaster’s prescriptions might give him a clue as to what the old fellow really thought of Lexton’s mysterious condition.

===Chinese===
他慢条斯理地拆开那只大号的方角淡紫色信封。里面是一张对折的便笺，还有一件附寄物：一只信封，上写着“处方”。

他扫了一眼那张便笺：

> 亲爱的伯威克医生：
> 您嘱我把兰开斯特医生的处方寄给您。您走后我刚好找到了。杰维斯今天下午觉得好些了，护士说，您要是忙，明天就不必来了。
> 您真挚的
> 艾薇·莱克斯顿

他望了望妻子。“是莱克斯顿太太写来的。她说她丈夫好些了，明天不必过去了。这倒省心！”

他随手抽出另一只信封里装的东西。兰开斯特医生的处方，或许能让他看出一线端倪——老先生对莱克斯顿那蹊跷的病，心里到底是怎么想的。

===Original===
“Hallo!” he exclaimed in a tone of extreme surprise, for what was written on a wide sheet of thin, common paper, folded in eight, ran:

> My own precious love (for that you are and always will be)﻿—
> Of course I quite see your point of view. Indeed I absolutely agree in a sense with every word that you have written to me. We have done wrong in allowing ourselves to love one another, and when I say “we” I really mean I Roger Gretorex, not you, Ivy Lexton. You were, you are, the purest and best woman I have ever known.
> I can swear before God that, had you been even moderately happy, I would have killed myself rather than have disturbed your peace. My only excuse, not for having loved you﻿—of that I am not at all ashamed﻿—but for having let you know that I loved you, is that when we first met you had begun to find how bitter a loveless life can be.
> You say you feel you ought never to come again to Ferry Place. I bow to your decision, dearest, and I will say that you are right in having come to that decision, even though it causes me agony. Thank you for saying I may still write to you, and that you will sometimes telephone to me.
> Yours devoted
> Roger Gretorex.

Berwick read the letter right through. Then he handed it to his wife.
