“Janey? I want you to tell me what you think of this! Both of the writer, I mean, and of the woman to whom this letter was written?”

===Chinese===
“咦！”他极度惊讶地叫出声来，因为那张薄薄的普通宽幅纸——折成八折的——上面写的是：

> 我最珍爱的人儿（你过去是、将来也永远是这样）——
> 当然，我完全明白你的想法。真的，你在信里对我说的每一句话，从某种意义上讲我都绝对赞同。我们不该听任自己相爱，这是错了的；而我说“我们”的时候，其实指的只是我，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，不是你，艾薇·莱克斯顿。你过去是、如今也是我所识得的最纯洁、最好的女人。
> 我可以在上帝面前起誓：哪怕你有过一丝一毫的幸福，我也宁愿自戕，决不去搅扰你的安宁。我唯一的辩解——不是为爱过你，这一点我毫不羞愧——而是为让你知道了我爱你，其缘由只是：我们初识的时候，你已开始尝到没有爱的生活是何等苦涩。
> 你说你觉得再也不该到费里街来了。我遵从你的决定，最亲爱的，而且我要说，你这样决定是对的，尽管它叫我痛苦万分。谢谢你允诺我仍可给你写信，还说你偶尔会打电话给我。
> 你矢志不渝的
> 罗杰·格雷托雷克斯

伯威克把信从头到尾读了一遍，然后递给了妻子。

“杰妮？我要你告诉我，你对这个怎么想！我是说，既评评写信的人，也评评这信写给的那个女人。”

===Original===
Slowly, with her husband closely watching her, and feeling, it must be admitted, ashamed of what she was doing, Janey Berwick read Roger Gretorex’s letter to Ivy Lexton right through.

Then she looked across at her husband, and her face bore an expression that a little surprised him. He had expected it to be filled with the wrath and disgust he felt himself.

“This is written with a man’s heart’s blood,” she said at last. “There must be more in this little Mrs. Lexton than you think, Angus. Surely this letter cannot be in answer to one sent by a silly, frivolous woman?”

“I wonder,” he said gloomily, “what their real relations have been. This letter might, of course, mean one of two things.”

===Chinese===
杰妮·伯威克慢悠悠地把罗杰·格雷托雷克斯写给艾薇·莱克斯顿的信通读了一遍，丈夫的眼睛紧盯着她，而她自己——必须承认——正为手头做的事感到羞愧。

随后她抬眼望着丈夫，脸上的神情倒叫他有几分诧异。他原以为那脸上会满是他自己也怀着的愤怒与憎恶。

“这信是用一个男人的心头血写成的。”她终于开口，“这位小小的莱克斯顿太太，内里一定比你以为的要多，安格斯（Angus）。这样一封信，总不会是回复一个愚蠢轻佻的女人的吧？”

“我真想晓得，”他阴郁地说，“他们俩到底是什么关系。这封信，当然，可能作两种解释。”

===Original===
She was reading the letter once more, slowly and carefully. At last she looked up. “I am inclined to think﻿—” then she stopped and exclaimed, “I don’t know what to think, Angus!”

“What were you going to say just now?” he asked quickly.

“I was going to say that I’m inclined to think that their friendship has not been innocent. That was what I was going to say; but even in these last few moments I’ve turned right round! Now I would say in all sincerity, my dear, that I think it very probable that there’s been nothing but passionate love on his side, and I suppose grateful affection on hers. She evidently doesn’t care for her husband; so much is quite clear.”

“No one would ever think so, from her way of speaking of him. The only time I’ve ever seen them together they seemed on the most affectionate terms. He was calling her ‘darling’ all the time, and she called him ‘dear old boy,’ and seemed genuinely very much worried about him.”

===Chinese===
她又把信慢慢地、细细地读了一遍。终于抬起头来。“我倾向于认为——”话到一半又收住，嚷道，“我不知道该怎么想，安格斯！”

“你刚才要说什么？”他赶紧问。

“我本想说我倾向于认为，他们的交情并非清白。我方才要说的就是这个；可就在这一会儿工夫里，我已经完全转过弯来了！如今我可以一片真心地说，亲爱的，依我看极有可能，那不过是他一腔炽烈的爱，而她这边，我想只是感念的情意。她分明不爱她的丈夫；这一点是相当清楚的。”

“听她谈起丈夫的口吻，谁也不会这么想。我统共见过他们俩在一起一回，那样子倒是恩爱得很。他一口一个‘亲爱的’，她唤他‘老伙计’，而且看样子，是真为他着急得很。”

===Original===
“At any rate she’s now made up her mind to do the right thing,” said Janey Berwick gravely. “One can’t but honour her for that, when one remembers﻿—”

She smiled, a curious little smile.

“Yes, my dear? Out with it!”

“After all, it is very delightful to be loved,” she said softly, “and this poor young chap evidently adores her.”

===Chinese===
“不管怎样，她如今是打定主意要做正当事了。”杰妮·伯威克正色道，“想到这一点，就不能不敬重她，只要记得——”

她莞尔一笑，一个古怪的小小微笑。

“怎么讲，亲爱的？有话直说！”

“说到底，被人爱着终归是件美事。”她柔声说，“而这个可怜的年轻人，分明把她奉若神明。”

===Original===
“Now comes a difficult question: what am I to do with this letter? I wonder if I ought to send it back to her﻿—”

“If I were you, I wouldn’t send it back to her. If she’s the sort of woman you’ve described her to be, it’s quite likely she’ll never discover that she sent it you by mistake.”

“Ought I to put it in the fire?”

“I don’t think you ought to do that. It doesn’t belong to you. You’ve no right to destroy it. Wait a day or two, dearest, and see what happens. She may ask you if you have got the letter? Then you can give it back to her. I’ll keep it if you like, Angus. We’ll put it in an envelope and I’ll address it to myself. If I keep it in the secret drawer of my old desk over there, only you and I will know where it is.”

===Chinese===
“现在来了一道难题：这封信我怎么处置？我不晓得该不该把它退回给她——”

“我要是你，就不退给她。她若是像你描述的那种性子的女人，多半一辈子也发觉不了，自己错把信寄给了你。”

“我该把它烧了吗？”

“我看你不该那么办。它不属于你，你无权销毁它。等上一两天再说，最亲爱的，看看情形。她兴许会问起你是否收到了这封信？那时你再还她不迟。你要是愿意，就交我收着，安格斯。咱们把它装进一只信封，写上我自己的名字收。搁进那边我那张旧书桌的暗屉里，天底下就只有你我两人知道它在哪儿。”

## VII / 七

===Original===
A long, long week went by, and it was now the evening of the 16th of November. Nurse Bradfield had been out for an hour after lunch, and while she was out Ivy had “looked after” Jervis. She made a point of doing this at some time of her day, though it was always overfull.

Being both good humoured and good natured, Nurse Bradfield fell in easily with any plan proposed by her patient’s wife. She had become very fond of Jervis Lexton, and, though aware of Ivy Lexton’s selfishness and innate frivolity and levity, she was yet attracted, in spite of herself, by the younger woman’s beauty, and what was in very truth an exceptional charm of manner, and what some of Ivy’s friends called her cheeriness.

Nurse Bradfield would have been surprised indeed could she have looked into Ivy Lexton’s mind, and seen how often and how anxiously that mind was occupied with herself.

Often the nurse would be touched and gratified by the consideration with which she was treated, and her comfort studied. It was no wonder that she, on her side, never even thought of insisting on her right to a certain amount of rest and exercise. She was no longer a young woman. She had few friends in London, and this day nursing job with a pleasant young couple was an agreeable interlude in her often anxious and hardworking life.

===Chinese===
漫长、漫长的一个星期过去了，眼下已是 11 月 16 日的晚上。午饭后，布拉德菲尔德护士（Nurse Bradfield）出去了一个钟头，趁她不在，艾薇便「照看」了杰维斯一番。尽管她每天的日子总是排得满满当当，她总要在一天之中抽出某个时辰做成这件事。

布拉德菲尔德护士性情和气、心地又好，病人太太提出的任何安排，她都乐于顺从。她已非常喜欢杰维斯·莱克斯顿；虽然她也看得出艾薇·莱克斯顿自私，天性轻佻、浮浅，却仍不由自主地为这位年轻女子的美貌所吸引——为那份确乎出众罕见的仪态风韵所吸引，也为艾薇的某些朋友所称道的她那股快活劲儿所吸引。

倘若布拉德菲尔德护士能看透艾薇·莱克斯顿的心思，看见那颗心里多么频繁、多么急切地盘算着她自己，她准会大吃一惊。

护士常常为病家待她的周到体谅、对她舒适安适的处处留意而感动、而欣慰。无怪乎她这一方面，从未想到要坚持自己理应享有的一定量休息与锻炼的权利。她已不再年轻，在伦敦朋友寥寥，而这桩陪护一对和气年轻夫妇的日班差事，在她那常怀忧虑、辛勤劳碌的生活里，倒是一段惬意的插曲。

===Original===
So on this early afternoon Ivy and Jervis had what her patient afterwards weakly described to his nurse as a quiet, nice little time together.

Then Ivy had gone out to a bridge-party, and now she had just come in, leaving herself barely time enough to dress and go out again.

To the young day-maid who hovered, timidly, admiringly, about her, Mrs. Lexton sadly expressed her regret at being in too great a hurry to see Mr. Lexton, even for a minute.

Hurry was the word today. She was hurrying over her dressing as she never hurried before, and, while she made up with feverish haste, there was a strange look on her lovely face. She even noticed that she did not look “quite the thing,” as she gazed at herself in the looking-glass, and she tried, but it was a failure, to smile, reassuringly, at herself.

===Chinese===
于是这天午后刚过，艾薇和杰维斯便共度了一段时光——事后病人虚弱地向护士描述说，那是一段安安静静、和和美美的小聚。

随后艾薇出门赴一场桥牌会，此刻她刚刚回来，只给自己留下勉强够穿衣打扮再出门的时间。

年轻的日班女佣怯生生地、满怀倾慕地在她身旁打转，莱克斯顿太太满脸戚容地向她表示遗憾：自己赶得太急，连见莱克斯顿先生一面——哪怕一分钟——都来不及了。

今天，一个「赶」字当头。她梳妆打扮从未这样匆忙过；当她急急忙忙地上妆时，那张可爱的脸上带着一种异样的神情。她甚至察觉，镜中的自己看上去「不大对劲」，便试着对自己投去一个宽解的微笑——却徒劳无功。

===Original===
She had turned away from the dressing-table and had just slipped her frock over her head, when there was a knock at the bedroom door, and Nurse Bradfield came in.

“I feel anxious about Mr. Lexton,” she said in a worried voice. “I don’t like his colour. Will you phone for the doctor, Mrs. Lexton? I hate leaving Mr. Lexton, even for a moment!”

Ivy of course murmured a word of assent. Then it was as if her heart bounded in her breast. Had it come at last﻿—her order of release?

She felt a spasm of terror shake her being. Also a sensation of abject fear of hard-faced, cold-mannered Dr. Berwick.

===Chinese===
她从梳妆台前转过身去，刚把连衣裙往头上一套，卧室门上便响起敲门声，布拉德菲尔德护士走了进来。

「我很担心莱克斯顿先生，」她忧心忡忡地说，「他的脸色不好。请您打电话请医生来，好吗，莱克斯顿太太？我不愿撇下莱克斯顿先生，哪怕一刻也不愿意！」

艾薇自然低声应了一句。随即，她的心仿佛在胸膛里猛地一跳。它终于来了么——她的开释令？

她感到一阵恐怖的痉挛震彻全身；还有一阵卑怯的畏惧——对那个面色冷硬、举止冰冷的伯威克医生（Dr. Berwick）的畏惧。

===Original===
This morning she had discovered the envelope containing the prescriptions which she believed she had sent the doctor a few days ago. And as she stared at it, puzzled, she suddenly remembered﻿—remembered, with a sharp stab of dismay, thrusting Gretorex’s letter into an empty envelope which she herself, in the early days of Jervis’s illness, acting on Dr. Lancaster’s fussy advice, had marked “Prescriptions.” Fool, fool that she had been!

There was that in the lovely, sensitive face of her patient’s wife that caused the nurse to run forward and put her arms round her.

“Don’t be so upset, Mrs. Lexton! We’ll have the doctor round in a few minutes. Though he’s not my sort, Dr. Berwick is very clever, and maybe he’ll think of something to bring him round﻿—quick! But do please now phone at once!” And she almost ran out of the room.

===Chinese===
今天早上，她发现了那只装处方的信封——她原以为几天前就已寄给医生了。她纳闷地盯着它看，忽然想起来了——想起来自己是怎样把格雷托雷克斯那封信塞进了一只空信封；杰维斯刚生病的那些日子，她听信兰开斯特医生（Dr. Lancaster）絮絮叨叨的叮嘱，亲手在那信封上标了「处方」二字。她真是傻瓜，十足的傻瓜！

病人的妻子那张敏感可爱的脸上颇有某种神色，引得护士抢步上前，伸出双臂搂住了她。

「别这么难过，莱克斯顿太太！几分钟之内我们就能请医生过来。伯威克医生虽然不合我的脾气，人却非常精明，也许他能想出什么法子让他好起来——快！不过现在请千万马上打电话！」说着她几乎是跑着出了房门。

===Original===
But Ivy did not go over to the side of the bed where stood the telephone. Instead, she went and sat down again in front of the pretty dressing-table. She shrank with terror from the thought of sending for Dr. Berwick “all in a hurry, like this.”

Oh! What had made her give that large, that dangerous, dose of the﻿—the stuff, to Jervis today? He had felt weak, weak and what he oddly called “spent.” So she had mixed him a stiff brandy and soda. And the doing of that had seemed such a good opportunity for﻿ ﻿… Ivy did not end the sentence, even to herself.

Again, why wasn’t Roger Gretorex in London? What a fool she had been to let him go for a long weekend down to Sussex to his tiresome mother. There was such support in his unquestioning love, in his adoring devotion, especially as he no longer asked her, with pleading, ardent, burning words of longing, to go to that horrid little house in Ferry Place.

At last, slowly, and with dragging steps, she went over to the telephone and rang up the doctor’s house.

===Chinese===
可是艾薇没有走向摆放电话机的床边，反而走回去，在那张漂亮的梳妆台前重新坐下。一想到要「像这样急急忙忙地」去请伯威克医生，她便惊惧畏缩。

哦！今天她究竟是中了什么邪，竟给杰维斯服下那么大、那么危险的一剂——那东西？他一直觉得虚弱，虚弱得——用他自己古怪的说法——「耗尽了」。于是她便给他调了一杯浓烈的白兰地苏打。而做这件事，似乎正好是个再好不过的机会，可以……这句话艾薇没有说完，连对自己也没有。

再说，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯为什么不在伦敦？她真是糊涂，竟放他回萨塞克斯他那位烦人的母亲那儿，去度一个长长的周末。他那份不问缘由的爱情、那份崇拜般的痴心，是何等的依靠；尤其如今他已不再用恳求的、炽烈的、灼人的渴望之词，求她去费里街那所讨厌的小房子了。

终于，她缓缓地、拖着沉重的脚步走到电话机前，拨通了医生家的电话。

===Original===
To her relief it was a woman’s voice, kindly, gentle, which answered.

“My husband is away. He won’t be back till tomorrow morning. Mr. Lexton less well? I’m so sorry. I’ll have a message sent round at once to Dr. Singleton, who always takes over Dr. Berwick’s work. He only lives two doors off, and his telephone is unluckily out of order.”

Ivy waited for what seemed to the other a very long time.

“Are you sure he’ll be able to come at once?” she asked.

===Chinese===
使她宽慰的是，接电话的是一个女人声音，和蔼而温柔。

「我丈夫外出了，要到明天早上才回来。莱克斯顿先生病得更重了？真遗憾。我马上派人送信给辛格尔顿医生（Dr. Singleton），他一向接管伯威克医生的诊务。他就住在隔两个门的地方，只是不巧他的电话出了故障。」

艾薇等了片刻——在对方听来，这片刻着实漫长。

「您能肯定他马上就能来吗？」她问。

===Original===
There came the slightly impatient answer, “I can’t be sure of that, for Dr. Singleton may be out. But he’ll come as soon as he can.”

“We want someone at once,” and the voice sounded so sad, so woeful, that Mrs. Berwick, at the other end of the telephone, felt ashamed of the suspicion she had harboured about young Mrs. Lexton ever since the night she had read Roger Gretorex’s love-letter. It was, however, a suspicion she had kept to herself. Even if these two had been lovers, they were so no longer. And in any case it was none of her business.

But what was this Mrs. Lexton was saying?

“There’s a doctor living in Duke of Kent Mansion. I think I’d better try and get him, don’t you? My husband is so very ill. Nurse is quite frightened!”

Without waiting for Mrs. Berwick’s assent, Ivy hung up the receiver.

===Chinese===
回答里带着些许不耐烦：「这我可说不准，辛格尔顿医生也许出门了。不过他会尽快赶来的。」

「我们马上就得有位医生，」那声音听上去如此悲戚、如此哀恸，以致电话另一端的伯威克太太（Mrs. Berwick）为自己心中久藏的疑心感到羞愧——自从那天夜里她读到罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的情书以来，她便对这位年轻的莱克斯顿太太怀着这份疑心。不过，这疑心她一直深藏心底。这两个人即便曾是一对情人，如今也不再是了。况且无论如何，这与她毫不相干。

可是这位莱克斯顿太太这是在说什么？

「有一位医生就住在肯特公爵大厦里。我想我不如去请他来，您说呢？我丈夫病得这么重，护士都吓坏了！」

没等伯威克太太表示同意，艾薇便挂上了听筒。

===Original===
She felt very much less afraid than she had felt just now. Surely fate was playing into her hands? She told herself that it was really a most fortunate thing that Dr. Berwick happened to be away.

She went out into the hall and listened. But no sounds were coming from the sick man’s room.

Timorously she called out, “Nurse!”

She was afraid to open Jervis’s bedroom door, for she was determined not to see him. To do so, she told herself, would do him no good, and only make her feel miserable.

===Chinese===
她觉得自己比方才害怕轻多了。这难道不是命运正在暗中帮她么？她告诉自己，伯威克医生恰好外出，实在是一件再凑巧不过的事。

她走到外面的门厅里侧耳细听。病人房里没有传出一点声息。

她怯生生地喊了一声：「护士！」

她不敢推开杰维斯卧室的门，因为她打定了主意不去看他。她对自己说，那样做于他毫无益处，只会让她自己心里难受。

===Original===
The kitchen door, situated some way off, at the end of the long corridor, swung open, and the nurse appeared, a jug of boiling water in her hand.

“I’m coming,” she called out, “I’m coming, Mr. Lexton. Is the doctor here already?”

“Dr. Berwick has gone away till tomorrow morning. Shall we try and get the doctor who lives in number **1A**, downstairs?”

“Oh, I don’t think Dr. Berwick would like us to do that! He was so disagreeable about that nice Dr. Gretorex. Surely Dr. Berwick has someone who takes his patients when he’s away? I never heard of such a thing as a doctor leaving his patients unattended! But I’ve thought of something to give Mr. Lexton that may ease the dreadful pain. He seems a little better now.”

===Chinese===
厨房的门开在长廊尽头的老远处，这时一下推开了，护士走了出来，手里提着一壶滚沸的水。

「我来了，」她扬声应道，「我来了，莱克斯顿先生。医生已经到了吗？」

「伯威克医生外出了，要到明天早上才回来。我们不如去请住在楼下**1A**的那位医生，好不好？」

「哦，我看伯威克医生不会乐意我们这么做！为了那位挺好的格雷托雷克斯医生，他发的脾气还少吗。伯威克医生外出时，总该有人替他照看病人吧？医生撇下病人不管，这种事我还从来没听说过！不过我想出一样东西可以给莱克斯顿先生用，也许能缓一缓那可怕的疼痛。他现在似乎好一点儿了。」

===Original===
“I’m glad of that,” said Ivy fervently. And she was glad. It hurt her, made her feel wretched, when she had time to remember it, to think of poor Jervis suffering.

As time had gone on, Nurse Bradfield had liked Dr. Berwick less and less. He had such a short, unpleasant way with him. Also, she was too experienced not to have quickly seen that he was both puzzled and irritated by his patient’s lack of reaction to his treatment. Once or twice she had thrown out a feeler about this. But he had rebuffed her, almost rudely, while yet giving her strict instructions never to leave her patient alone, and always to administer herself the food and the medicines prescribed. This had surprised, and even offended her.

Not only had the nurse become fond, in a way, of beautiful Mrs. Lexton, but for Jervis himself she had now almost a tender feeling. He was such a real gentleman, giving as little trouble as he could, and even when in sharp pain invariably patient and good-humoured.

===Chinese===
「那我很高兴。」艾薇恳切地说。她也确实高兴。一想到可怜的杰维斯在受苦，她心里便觉刺痛，便觉难过——每当得空想起来的时候。

随着时间一天天过去，布拉德菲尔德护士对伯威克医生的好感越来越少。他待人接物总是那么简慢生硬、不客气。再者，她经验太丰富了，不可能看不出：病人对他的治疗毫无反应，这既让他困惑，又让他恼怒。她曾有一两次就此旁敲侧击地探过口风，却被他近乎粗暴地顶了回来，却又对她下了严格的指示：绝不可让病人独处，一切饮食和处方药物必须由她亲手经管。这令她惊讶，甚至觉得受了冒犯。

这位护士不仅已对美丽的莱克斯顿太太生出几分喜爱，对杰维斯本人如今更是几乎怀着一种柔情。他是那样一位真正的绅士，尽量不给人添麻烦；哪怕剧痛发作，也总是耐心而和悦。

===Original===
“I suppose you’d like me to stay in tonight?” asked Ivy nervously.

Nurse Bradfield hesitated.

“I don’t think you need, really. It isn’t as if you could do anything, Mrs. Lexton? I can manage quite well; and if I think it necessary I can always send cook for the doctor in the flat downstairs.”

===Chinese===
「我想您大概愿意我今晚留在家里吧？」艾薇紧张地问。

布拉德菲尔德护士迟疑了一下。

「我看不必，真的。反正您在这儿也帮不上什么忙，不是吗，莱克斯顿太太？我一个人完全照看得过来；倘若觉得有必要，我随时可以打发厨娘下楼去那套公寓请医生。」

===Original===
So Ivy went off, with a sensation of intense relief, to a theatre-party, composed of a young married woman of much her own age and two men. Her own special escort was a good-looking bachelor whom she had met since her return to London, and with whom she spent a great deal of what she called her spare time.

She determined to banish Jervis from her mind, and, in quite a short time, she succeeded. After all, he was “a little better,” and Nurse Bradfield was kindness itself.

Ivy had abstained from saying to which theatre she was going, and after an amusing evening spent in laughing at a really funny farce, the four went on to supper at the Carlton.

Her new man friend drove her home at almost one in the morning, and she lingered for quite a long while in the deserted hall of the Duke of Kent Mansion, bidding him farewell. When at last he went off she felt quite “good,” for she had only allowed him one kiss.

===Chinese===
于是艾薇怀着如释重负的轻松，出门赴一场看戏的聚会去了；同行的有一位年纪与她相仿的年轻已婚妇女和两位男士。专门陪伴她的是一位相貌英俊的单身汉，她回伦敦后结识的；她自称闲暇时光的一大部分，便是同他一起消磨的。

她打定主意把杰维斯从心里驱走，而且没过多久便如愿了。毕竟他已经「好一点儿了」，再说布拉德菲尔德护士又是天底下再好不过的人。

艾薇没有说明要去哪家剧院；这个夜晚过得有滋有味，一出真正逗笑的闹剧引得他们笑了又笑，随后四人又到卡尔顿饭店（the Carlton）去吃夜宵。

将近凌晨一点，她那位新交的男友才开车送她回家；在肯特公爵大厦空寂的门厅里，她又流连了好一阵子才与他道别。等他终于走了，她觉得自己相当「乖」，因为她只容许了他一个吻。

===Original===
Lightly she ran up the stairs, for the lift stopped working at midnight. But when she reached the landing outside her front door, Ivy Lexton did not at once put her latchkey in the lock. Indeed, she waited for quite a long time. At last, however, she did put the key in the lock, and slowly she turned it.

Then she gave a stifled cry of surprise, for Nurse Bradfield was sitting in the hall, waiting for her.

There was a look of great distress, almost of shame, on the nurse’s kind face. She got up, and looked straight into the now terrified eyes of the merrymaker.

“I’ve bad news for you﻿—very bad news, Mrs. Lexton.”

===Chinese===
电梯午夜就停了，她轻捷地跑上楼梯。可是走到自家大门外的楼梯平台上时，艾薇·莱克斯顿并没有立刻把弹簧锁钥匙插进锁孔。说真的，她等了足足好一阵子。不过最后，她终究还是把钥匙插进锁孔，缓缓地转动了它。

随即她发出一声压低的惊呼——布拉德菲尔德护士正坐在门厅里等她。

护士那张善良的脸上带着极度悲戚、几乎是羞惭的神情。她站起身来，直直地望进这位夜游欢归者此刻惊恐万状的眼睛。

「我有个坏消息要告诉您——非常坏的消息，莱克斯顿太太。」

===Original===
She waited, hoping the other would say something that would imply she understood what that bad news must be.

But Ivy remained silent, staring at Nurse Bradfield with terror-filled, dilated eyes.

“Mr. Lexton took a very serious turn for the worse about half-past ten. I sent at once for the doctor downstairs, but he was out; and﻿—and﻿—” she did not finish her sentence. “I don’t think anything could have saved him. His heart gave way﻿—that’s what it was! He looks so young, so boyish, so peaceful.”

The tears came into her eyes. “Would you like to see him?”

===Chinese===
她顿了顿，指望对方说句什么，表明她已明白那坏消息必然是什么。

可是艾薇一言不发，睁着一双充满恐怖、瞳孔放大的眼睛，直瞪着布拉德菲尔德护士。

「十点半左右，莱克斯顿先生的病情急剧恶化。我立即派人去请楼下那位医生，可他外出了；还有——还有——」她的话没有说完。「我想无论什么也救不了他了。他的心脏衰竭了——就是这么回事！他看上去那么年轻，那么孩子气，那么安详。」

泪水涌上了她的眼睛。「您想去看看他吗？」

===Original===
“Oh no! I﻿—I couldn’t!”

The newly-made widow burst into tears, and the older woman led her tenderly to her bedroom, and helped her to undress.

“I’ll never forget how kind you were to my poor darling﻿—and to me, too, nurse. I hope to be able to prove my gratitude some day,” whispered Ivy, after the other had tucked her up in bed.

Nurse Bradfield was touched. She forgot how selfish she had secretly thought little Mrs. Lexton’s conduct had been, in practically insisting on going out this evening. She only remembered thenceforth her pretty ways, her sweet manner to her poor husband, and the easy good nature which always made her willing, when she happened to be at home, to arrange some little treat for her husband’s kind nurse.

===Chinese===
「哦，不！我——我做不到！」

这位新寡顿时泪如雨下；年长的女人温柔地把她领进卧室，服侍她脱衣就寝。

「我永远忘不了您待我可怜的宝贝是多么好心——对我也是一样，护士。我希望有朝一日能报答您的恩情。」等对方替她掖好被角，艾薇悄声说。

布拉德菲尔德护士感动了。她忘记了自己曾暗自觉得，这位小小的莱克斯顿太太今晚执意非出门不可，是多么自私。此后她只记得她那些可人的姿态，她待可怜丈夫的温柔体贴，还有她那随和的好脾气——每逢碰巧在家，她总乐意为丈夫这位好心的护士安排些小小的款待。

---

===Original===
That night Ivy lay for a long, long while with eyes wide open in the darkness. What had just happened filled her with a kind of awe. She had not known how easy and simple is the passage from life to death.

She reminded herself how very kind, how good a pal, she had always been to poor Jervis, and how happy he had been these last two months, owing to Miles Rushworth.

Dear, delightful, generous Miles Rushworth! The thought of him brought a rush of joy, as well as comfort, in its train.

At last a great peace descended upon her. All the terrors which had assailed her during those infrequent moments when she had been, by some strange chance, alone, during her husband’s illness, had vanished as if they had never been.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，艾薇睁着眼睛在黑暗中躺了很久、很久。刚刚发生的事使她心中充满一种敬畏。她原先并不知道，从生到死的通道竟是这般轻易、这般简单。

她提醒自己，她待可怜的杰维斯一向多么好心、一向是个多好的伙伴；这两个月来，多亏了迈尔斯·拉什沃思，他又过得多么快活。

亲爱的、讨人喜欢的、慷慨大度的迈尔斯·拉什沃思！一想起他，一阵欢愉便随之涌上心头，还带来了安慰。

终于，一片巨大的安宁笼罩了她。丈夫患病期间，每逢不知怎么独自一人待着的那些难得时刻，曾向她袭来的种种恐怖，都已烟消云散，仿佛从不曾存在过。

===Original===
As she looked back she shivered at the thought of how frightened she had sometimes felt. And yet, after all, she knew now that there had been nothing to be really frightened about.

Roger Gretorex had been right as to what he had said, on that evening she still remembered so well. At the time she had thought it horrid of him to imagine such a thing as that﻿—she let what he had suggested stay undefined. No need surely to put it into ugly words, even to herself. All the same, she did bring herself to face the comforting fact that, if rather horrible, it was certainly true. A great many more people are undoubtedly helped out of life than stupid, unimaginative folk suppose. As to those who﻿—well﻿—help them to leave a world which has no further use for them, they certainly, as Roger had declared, “got away with it,” often with marvellous success.

True, that disagreeable Dr. Berwick had been puzzled﻿—he had said as much to her. But she had got on much better with him during the last couple of days, since, in fact, he had seen that she really could not endure the sight of poor Jervis’s sufferings.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
回首往事，想到自己有时竟是那样担惊受怕，她不由得打了个寒噤。然而说到底，她如今知道了，本来就没有什么真正可怕的事。

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯说的话是对的——就在她至今记得清清楚楚的那个晚上。当时她还觉得他竟会设想出那样一件事，实在可怖——他暗示的那件事，她就任它模模糊糊地悬着，不加落实。没有必要把它说成难听的字眼，哪怕对自己也罢。尽管如此，她还是让自己直面了那个宽慰人心的事实：这事虽然相当可怕，却千真万确。毫无疑问，被人「送出」人世的，要比那些愚蠢而缺乏想象力的人所设想的多得多。至于那些——嗯——帮人离开这个对他们再无用处的世界的人，正如罗杰所断言的，他们「侥幸脱身」了，而且往往成功得出奇。

不错，那位讨人嫌的伯威克医生曾大惑不解——他或多或少对她这样说过。可最后这两天，她同他相处得好多了；因为事实上，他已看出她实在不忍目睹可怜的杰维斯所受的苦楚。……

===Original===
The word “death,” which means so much to the great majority of men and women, meant very little to Ivy Lexton. Indeed, she felt, as she lay there in the darkness, that this man who had been her lover-comrade had only gone away out of her life on a long journey﻿—a journey from which, however, he was never to return. The fact that she had planned that journey, that it was owing to her direct action that he had gone from the world he found on the whole so pleasant, would and must, not only remain hidden, but in time be forgotten even by herself.

In a way, she was forgetting it already as she turned to her cloudless, sunny future﻿—to the delightful existence she would henceforth lead, first as the fiancée, and then as the wife, of Miles Rushworth.

Run straight? Of course she would run straight! For one thing, Rushworth wasn’t the sort of man with whom it would be safe to run crooked. For all his odd ideas, and to Ivy his ideas as to morality were indeed singular, she realised that he had his weather eye very much open. She would henceforth have to be what to herself she vaguely called “a good woman.” But then, how easy it is to be a good woman when one has a hundred thousand a year!

===Chinese===
「死」这个字眼，对世间绝大多数男女意义重大，对艾薇·莱克斯顿却意义甚微。真的，躺在黑暗里，她觉得这个曾是她的情人兼伙伴的男人，不过是离开她的生活作一次长途旅行去了——只是这趟旅行，他是永远不会回来的。而这次旅行是她一手筹划的，他之所以离开这个他总体觉得相当惬意的人世，正是出于她的直接行动——这个事实必将也必须不仅始终隐藏，而且终将连她自己也一并忘却。

从某种意义上说，她已经正在把它忘掉，因为她已转向自己那万里无云、阳光灿烂的未来——转向她从此将要过的那种美妙生活：先做迈尔斯·拉什沃思的未婚妻，然后做他的妻子。

走正路？她当然会走正路！首先，拉什沃思不是那种可以放心大胆同他耍歪的人。尽管他有些古怪的念头——在艾薇看来，他的道德观念确实别具一格——她明白他那只警戒的眼睛始终睁得大大的。她从此得做一个她自己含糊地称作「好女人」的女人。不过话说回来，一个人一年有十万进项，做个好女人又是何等容易啊！

## VIII / 八

===Original===

After a good night of deep, healthy sleep, Ivy Lexton awoke. She sat up in bed and then, all at once, she remembered.﻿ ﻿…

Her first sensation was one of intense, almost painfully acute, relief. True, she would still have, for just a little while, to play a part. But playing a part was to her second nature. Now and again, but very rarely, in moments of suspense, and now and again in moments of panic, there would appear before a pair of astonished eyes another woman altogether to the everyday Ivy.

She slid out of her comfortable warm bed and softly opened the door of her bedroom, to see that the grandfather’s clock in the hall marked a quarter past nine.

The kindly, fussy old cook came out of her kitchen, and when she saw the slender figure, clad in diaphanous pale pink ninon trimmed with lace, standing half-in half-out of the door, she hurried down the passage and whispered, “Go back to bed, ma’am! I won’t be a minute in getting you a cup of tea. I scarcely slept a wink all night, I was that upset. Mr. Lexton was such a nice young gentleman.”

===Chinese===

酣酣沉沉、有益健康的一夜好眠之后，艾薇·莱克斯顿醒了过来。她在床上坐起身，随即，一下子全想起来了。……

她的第一重感受，是一种强烈的、几乎尖锐得叫人发痛的如释重负。不错，她还得分明再扮上一小阵子的戏。可扮戏于她本是第二天性。只是间或——极难得地——在悬虑不决的时刻，在惊慌失措的时刻，会当着一双惊愕的眼睛，倏然现出另一个女人来，与平日的艾薇判若两人。

她从舒适暖和的床上溜下来，轻轻推开卧室的门，看见门厅里那座落地大座钟指着九点一刻。

心地和善、爱瞎操心的老厨娘从厨房里出来，瞧见那纤瘦的身影——一身缀着花边的浅粉红尼农绸（ninon）薄衫，半在门里半在门外——便急急忙忙顺着走廊赶来，低声道：「快回床上去，太太！我这就去给您沏杯茶，一会儿就好。我一夜几乎没合眼，心里难过得不得了。莱克斯顿先生是多好的一个年轻先生啊。」

===Original===

“Thank you very, very much, cook!”

Tears, genuine tears, rose to Ivy’s eyes. What cook said was so true﻿—Jervis had been “such a nice young gentleman,” especially during this painful illness. It seemed so strange to think of him as gone, forever, from a world which on the whole had treated him so kindly, and of which he had been so contented a denizen. Ivy did not put the thoughts which haunted her mind in those words, but that was the purport of them.

After she had had her breakfast, she began to feel oddly restless, and so, earlier than was usual with her, she got up and dressed. Nurse Bradfield, it appeared, was still asleep.

The newly-made widow hesitated as to what she should wear. Finally she decided on a pretty black georgette frock she had bought from a friend who had started a profitable little business in French models, some of which she cleverly managed to smuggle over from Paris.

===Chinese===

「多谢，多谢你了，厨娘！」

泪水，真真切切的泪水，涌上了艾薇的眼眶。厨娘说的字字是实——杰维斯确是「多好的一个年轻先生」，尤其在这样一场痛苦的病中。想到他竟已离开人世，永远地离开了，而这世界待他总算宽厚，他又是那样一个安土乐居的居民，未免叫人觉得离奇。艾薇并没有把心头萦绕的念头照这样措辞，但那些念头的要旨便是如此。

吃过早餐，她开始感到一种说不出的烦乱不安，于是起了床、穿好衣裳——比她平素的时辰都早。布拉德菲尔德护士看样子还睡着。

新寡的妇人为该穿什么衣裳踌躇了一番，最后选定了一条漂亮的黑乔其纱连衣裙——那是从一位朋友手里买来的。那朋友做起了法国新款时装的小买卖，生意颇为红火，有些货色她还能巧妙地从巴黎私运过来。

===Original===

After she had put it on, Ivy looked at the reflection of herself in the long narrow panel of looking-glass set in the wall at right angles from the window. Yes, the dress was charming, and looked just right.

She ran across to a walnut-wood chest, and took out of it the hat she had bought yesterday. For the first time, since she had come into the flat last night, she smiled. The little hat was so chic, really chic! And it made her look so﻿—well, why not say it to herself?﻿—so absolutely lovely.

Slowly, reluctantly, she took off the hat, and then she went into the drawing-room.

The blinds were still drawn down. How strange! Then she remembered why they were drawn down.

===Chinese===

穿上身之后，艾薇对着嵌在墙上、与窗户成直角的那面狭长穿衣镜里的自己端详了一番。是啊，这条裙子真好看，穿起来正合适。

她奔到胡桃木衣柜前，取出昨天买的那顶帽子。自昨夜回到这套公寓以来，她头一回笑了。这顶小帽真别致，是真正的别致！而且它衬得她那么——嗯，干吗不对自己直说呢？——那么绝对地可爱。

她慢吞吞地、老大不情愿地摘下帽子，然后走进了客厅。

百叶窗还垂着。多奇怪啊！随即她想起了垂下它们的缘故。

===Original===

She wandered about the room, feeling just a little dazed. Should she telegraph for Roger Gretorex? It was so stupid of his mother to have given up the telephone. No one could be so poor as that; it was just meanness and affectation! But if she wired she knew he would come back at once. She also knew that she could trust him to take off her shoulders all worrying, maybe even unpleasant, arrangements.

And yet the fact that she was now a widow would certainly make Roger “tiresome.” So unfortunately certain was this that she felt it better to leave him alone, at any rate for the present. Also she was a little afraid of seeing him just now. After all, owing to his being a doctor, he had such an uncanny knowledge of﻿—of poisons, and of their effect on the human body.

She sat down; then she got up again, and at last she began moving about restlessly.

Suddenly she told herself that she might as well sit down and write to Miles Rushworth. It could be quite a short letter. He would of course remember that in her very last letter she had said that poor Jervis was worse, and that she was feeling anxious.

===Chinese===

她在房间里踱来踱去，只觉得有几分恍恍惚惚。要不要拍电报叫罗杰·格雷托雷克斯来？他母亲把电话给拆了，真是糊涂透顶。谁也不至于穷到那个地步；那不过是小气加做作！可她只要发个电报，就知道他准会立刻赶回来。她也知道，尽可以把一切烦心的、说不定还叫人难堪的安排，都托付给他，从自己肩上卸下去。

然而她如今成了寡妇，这个身份必定会使罗杰变得「讨厌」。这一点不幸而何其确凿，以至于她觉得还是别去招惹他为妙，至少眼下如此。再说，这会儿她也有点怕见他。毕竟他是当医生的，对——对毒药，对毒药在人体上起的作用，有着那样一种叫人发毛的学问。

她坐下；又站起来；最后她开始焦躁地走来走去。

冷不丁地，她对自己说，不妨就坐下给迈尔斯·拉什沃思写封信。信可以写得相当简短。他当然还记得，她在上一封信里说过可怜的杰维斯病势加重了，说她心里很是焦虑。

===Original===

She wondered how long it takes for a letter to get to South Africa. And then with a sensation of relief came the thought that she could cable. It would be quite natural for her to do that as, after all, her husband had been in Rushworth’s employment.

She went over to the writing-table, and, sitting down, drew a telegraph form towards her. She would write it out, and then take it herself to the post office. She didn’t feel, somehow, like sending a cable to Rushworth over the telephone. That is the worst of living in a flat. Everything one says may be overheard, especially from the hall.

But instead of taking up a pen, she put her elbows on the table and gazed in front of her.

There had suddenly come over her a most unexpected sensation of loneliness. Oh, if she had one good man friend who wasn’t in love with her, and who would help her through the next few days! She did so shrink from﻿—from everything. Laying her head on the table, she began to sob with self-pity.

===Chinese===

她不知道一封信寄到南非要多久。随后她想到可以拍电报，心里一宽。她这样做是再自然不过的事，毕竟她丈夫是在拉什沃思手下做事的。

她走到写字台前坐下，把一张电报单拉到面前。她要把它写好，然后亲自送到邮局去。不知怎么的，她不愿意用电话给拉什沃思发电报。住公寓就坏在这里：一个人说的话都可能被人听见，尤其是在门厅里。

然而她并没有拿起笔来，只是把手肘支在桌上，怔怔地望着前方。

一种全然出乎意料的孤独感蓦地袭上了她的心头。唉，倘若她有一个不迷恋她的正经男友，能帮她熬过往后这几天，那该多好！她对——对一切事情，实在畏缩得很。她把头伏在桌上，自伤自怜地啜泣起来。

===Original===

The door opened, and the nurse came in. Ivy looked up.

“I’m so miserable! So miserable! I don’t know what to do!”

“Don’t you worry about anything, Mrs. Lexton. Dr. Berwick will be round pretty soon. I telephoned to his house just now, and left a message for him. Mrs. Berwick was shocked to hear our dreadful news. She said she was expecting the doctor back any minute now. I expect Dr. Gretorex will be in some time today, too. Surely he’ll see to all the things that have to be done.”

“All the things?” Ivy looked timorously at Nurse Bradfield, and shivered.

===Chinese===

门开了，护士走了进来。艾薇抬起头。

「我心里好难受！好难受啊！我都不知道该怎么办了！」

「您什么都别操心，莱克斯顿太太。伯威克医生很快就会过来。我刚才往他家里打了电话，留了话。伯威克太太听到我们的噩耗吓了一跳。她说医生眼看着随时就到家了。我想格雷托雷克斯医生今天什么时候也会来。要办的那些事，他准会一手料理的。」

「那些事？」艾薇怯生生地望着布拉德菲尔德护士，打了个寒噤。

===Original===

The other saw her look of dismay. “Poor little thing,” she said to herself, “she’s not much more than a child, after all.”

Aloud she said, “I thought of going out presently. Not for long”﻿—fear had flashed into Ivy’s face﻿—“only just for a few minutes.”

She added kindly: “I shouldn’t try to write, if I were you, Mrs. Lexton. I’d just lie down and have a rest. I don’t suppose you’ve had much sleep?”

Ivy answered plaintively, “I lay awake all night. You see it was such a shock, nurse, such a dreadful shock,” and she thought that what she said was true.

===Chinese===

护士瞧见她那副惊惶的神色。「可怜的小人儿，」她暗自想道，「说到底，她也不过是个孩子。」

她出声说道：「我本想待会儿出去一趟。去不了多久」——恐惧在艾薇脸上一闪——「就那么几分钟。」

她又和善地添了一句：「我要是您，就不忙着写信，莱克斯顿太太。我就这么躺下歇一歇。您大概没怎么睡吧？」

艾薇哀声答道：「我一夜都醒着没合眼。您知道，那太突然了，护士，太可怕的一个打击。」而她觉得自己说的句句是真。

===Original===

In a way it had been a dreadful shock, for Ivy had never come face to face with death. She had been still a pupil at a fashionable school when her father had killed himself.

The nurse led her to the comfortable sofa. “You lie down here.”

Ivy obeyed, wondering why she felt as she did feel﻿—so thoroughly upset and unnerved.

She had been lying down perhaps ten minutes when she heard the now familiar knock of Dr. Berwick. She started up, and what natural colour she had left her cheeks. Angrily she told herself that it was stupid to feel frightened. There was nothing to be frightened about.

===Chinese===

从某种意义上说，那确是一个可怕的打击，因为艾薇从未与死亡打过照面。她父亲自尽的时候，她还是一所时髦学校里的学生。

护士把她领到那张舒适的长榻前。「您就在这儿躺下。」

艾薇依言躺下，心里纳罕自己何以会这样——这样彻底地心烦意乱、浑身瘫软。

她躺下大约十分钟光景，就听见了伯威克医生那如今已听熟了的叩门声。她霍地惊起，原本仅有的一点天然血色也从脸颊上褪了下去。她气恼地告诉自己，害怕是愚蠢的。没有什么好怕的。

===Original===

The door opened, and the doctor strode into the darkened room. He turned a frowning, preoccupied face on the newly-made widow. Then, when his eyes rested on the tear-stained little face, his expression softened.

“I’m more sorry than I can say that I happened to have been away all yesterday, Mrs. Lexton. I only came back this morning.”

Ivy began to cry, and again he felt touched by her evident distress.

“Sit down, Mrs. Lexton. Do sit down. I’m afraid you’ve had a terrible shock.”

===Chinese===

门开了，医生大步走进这间遮暗了的屋子。他把一张皱着眉、心事重重的脸转向这位新寡的妇人。待目光落到那张泪痕斑斑的小脸上，他的神情缓和了下来。

「昨天我偏偏一整天都不在，真是说不出的抱歉，莱克斯顿太太。我是今天早上才回来的。」

艾薇哭了起来，她那显而易见的悲痛又一次打动了他。

「坐下吧，莱克斯顿太太。快坐下。恐怕您受了一场可怕的打击。」

===Original===

“A dreadful, dreadful shock!” she sobbed, “I had no idea that Jervis was so ill.”

“Last time I was here he was certainly better,” he said quickly. “You thought him better too, didn’t you?”

“I did. I did indeed.”

She was trembling now, and though she was consciously playing a part, her emotion was still genuine.

===Chinese===

「可怕，太可怕的打击！」她抽泣道，「我根本没想到杰维斯病得这么重。」

「我上回来的时候，他确实见好。」他赶忙说，「您也觉得他好些了，是不是？」

「是的。我确实觉得。」

她这时浑身发着抖；虽说心里明白自己是在做戏，她的激动却仍是真情实感。

===Original===

She sat down on the sofa and the doctor drew up a chair and sat down too, a little to her surprise.

“If you feel up to it,” he said, “I want to ask you a few questions. I mean as to what happened yesterday?”

She dabbed her eyes with her handkerchief. “I can’t tell you very much, for I was out a great deal yesterday. But nurse never left Jervis, not for one moment. She’s been most awfully kind, and﻿—”

He cut her short, brusquely. “I know she’s a good old thing. What I want to know is whether Mr. Lexton received any visitor or visitors yesterday?”

===Chinese===

她在长榻上坐下，医生也拉过一把椅子坐了下来，这倒有点出乎她的意料。

「要是您撑得住，」他说，「我想问您几个问题。我是说，昨天都发生了些什么。」

她用手帕按了按眼睛。「我讲不出多少来，因为我昨天大部分时间都在外面。可护士一刻也没有离开过杰维斯，一分钟也没有。她好得没法说了，而且——」

他粗声粗气地打断了她。「我知道她是个好心的老好人。我想知道的是，莱克斯顿先生昨天有没有接待过来客？」

===Original===

“Visitors?” She looked at him in surprise. “Not that I know of. He was far too ill.”

In a tone which he strove to make light, he observed, “I thought that your husband might have seen Dr. Gretorex for a few moments.”

The colour rushed into her face.

“He can’t have seen him. Dr. Gretorex is in the country.” Then, a little confusedly, she added, “At least, I’m nearly sure that he is.”

===Chinese===

「来客？」她诧异地望着他。「据我所知没有。他病得实在太重了。」

他用一种刻意显得轻描淡写的口气说道：「我还以为，您丈夫兴许见着格雷托雷克斯医生一小会儿了呢。」

血色猛地涌上她的脸。

「他不可能见着他。格雷托雷克斯医生到乡下去了。」随后她又有点慌乱地补了一句：「至少，我差不多可以肯定他在乡下。」

===Original===

“Ah, well, then he can’t have come in, of course.”

A knock sounded on the door, and Nurse Bradfield came into the room.

Ivy welcomed her presence. Looking up into the kind face, now full of sympathy, she exclaimed:

“Dr. Berwick has been asking me if my husband saw anyone yesterday? But I’m quite sure Jervis wasn’t well enough to see anyone.”

===Chinese===

「啊，好吧，那他当然就不可能进来过了。」

门外响起叩门声，布拉德菲尔德护士走进屋来。

艾薇巴不得她在跟前。她仰望着那张此刻满怀同情的和善面孔，叫道：

「伯威克医生方才一直在问我，我丈夫昨天见过什么人没有？可我敢肯定，杰维斯的身体根本见不了客。」

===Original===

“Mr. Lexton only had one visitor,” said the nurse quickly, defensively, “and that was Dr. Gretorex.”

“I thought,” said the doctor, turning sharply on Ivy, “that you said just now that Dr. Gretorex was in the country?”

“He was to have been in the country, staying with his mother for a long weekend.”

There was no mistaking Ivy’s look of surprise. Not that she thought it mattered, one way or the other, whether Roger Gretorex had come in or not yesterday.

===Chinese===

「莱克斯顿先生只来过一位客人。」护士赶忙地、带着辩解的口气说，「那就是格雷托雷克斯医生。」

「我记得，」医生倏地转身逼视艾薇，「你方才不是说，格雷托雷克斯医生到乡下去了吗？」

「他本是该到乡下去的，要陪他母亲过一个长周末。」

艾薇脸上的惊愕做不得假。倒不是说她觉得罗杰·格雷托雷克斯昨天来没来过有什么要紧——无论来与不来，都无关紧要。

===Original===

“At what time was he here?” asked Dr. Berwick.

The nurse waited a moment. “I suppose it would have been about four o’clock. He didn’t mean to see Mr. Lexton.”

Said Dr. Berwick grimly to himself, “Oh, didn’t he?”

Nurse Bradfield went on, a little nervously: “He asked for Mrs. Lexton, and when he heard that she was coming in soon﻿—you said you wouldn’t be out long,” and she turned to Ivy﻿—“he said he would come in and wait. After he had been in the drawing-room about ten minutes, he rang for the maid and asked to see me. I told him I thought Mr. Lexton on the whole better, and then he inquired if Mr. Lexton would care to see him. He said he couldn’t stay long, as he had a train to catch﻿—”

===Chinese===

「他几点钟在这儿？」伯威克医生问。

护士顿了一顿。「大概是四点钟光景吧。他本来没打算见莱克斯顿先生。」

伯威克医生暗自狠狠地想：「哦，是没打算吗？」

布拉德菲尔德接着说下去，有点局促不安：「他本是要找莱克斯顿太太的，听说她很快就回来——您是说过出去不了多久，」她转向艾薇——「他便说干脆进来等一等。他在客厅里坐了约莫十分钟，就按铃叫女佣，说要见我。我告诉他，我看莱克斯顿先生总体上见好，他便问莱克斯顿先生愿不愿意见他。他说自己待不了多久，因为要赶一班火车——」

===Original===

Dr. Berwick said negligently, “Did you leave them alone together, nurse?”

“Yes, I did, doctor, for I knew they were great friends. Dr. Gretorex thought Mr. Lexton less well than the last time he had seen him. In fact, he saw a great change.”

“Did he tell you that?”

She replied quickly, “He told me that he thought him very far from well, and that he was distressed at the change he saw in him.”

===Chinese===

伯威克医生漫不经心地说：「你把他们俩单独留在了一起吗，护士？」

「是的，留下了，医生，因为我知道他们是极好的朋友。格雷托雷克斯医生觉得莱克斯顿先生比他上次见他时更不如了。说实在的，他看出变化很大。」

「他这么跟你说的？」

她赶忙回答：「他告诉我，他觉得他病得很重，还说眼见他变成这副样子，心里很难过。」

===Original===

“You never told me all that,” said Ivy plaintively.

“I ought to have done, Mrs. Lexton. But the truth is I was too upset, when Mr. Lexton took a turn for the worse, to remember anything.”

“I’m sure seeing Roger Gretorex for a few moments can’t have done him any harm,” said Ivy gently. “They were great friends.”

But as she made that commonplace remark, she flushed again, remembering Roger’s highfalutin’ letter﻿—what a fool she had been not to destroy it at once!

===Chinese===

「这些你从来没有告诉过我。」艾薇哀声说。

「我本该告诉您的，莱克斯顿太太。可实情是，莱克斯顿先生病势转危的时候，我心里乱得很，什么都记不得了。」

「我敢说，见罗杰·格雷托雷克斯那么一小会儿，不会对他有什么坏处。」艾薇温婉地说，「他们是极好的朋友。」

可说出这句平平常常的话时，她又脸红了，想起罗杰那封浮夸的信——当初没有立刻毁掉它，自己是何等愚蠢！

===Original===

“So I understood on that occasion when Dr. Gretorex, from my point of view, most improperly began to prescribe for him,” said the doctor, in a tone which, even to himself, sounded trenchantly ironic.

Meanwhile Nurse Bradfield, supposing that for the present the doctor had done with her, had turned towards the door.

“I should be obliged, nurse, if you would wait in the dining-room for a few moments. I should like to speak to you on my way out.”

“Certainly, Dr. Berwick.”

===Chinese===

「那回格雷托雷克斯医生——在我看来失当之至——擅自给病人开起方子来，我便是那么领教的。」医生说这话的口气，连他自己听来都尖利得近乎讥诮。

这时布拉德菲尔德护士以为医生眼下已用不着她了，便转身朝门口走去。

「劳驾，护士，请你在饭厅里稍候片刻。我出去的时候有话要同你说。」

「好的，伯威克医生。」

===Original===

The good woman told herself with a touch of contempt that he could have nothing of any moment to say to her. She had done her duty, and more than her duty as a day nurse, to poor Jervis Lexton.

As she shut the door, Dr. Berwick turned to his late patient’s widow.

“In the circumstances,” he said, in a slow, emphatic tone, “I am afraid, Mrs. Lexton, that there must be a postmortem.”

“A postmortem?” repeated Ivy falteringly. “What is a postmortem, Dr. Berwick?”

===Chinese===

这位善良的女人不无轻蔑地想，他对她不可能有什么要紧话可讲。对于可怜的杰维斯·莱克斯顿，她已尽了本分，尽了远超一个日班护士本分的本分。

她关上门，伯威克医生转向他那位亡故病人的遗孀。

「眼下的情形，」他以缓慢而郑重的口气说道，「恐怕，莱克斯顿太太，必须做一次验尸（postmortem）。」

「验尸？」艾薇结结巴巴地重复道，「验尸是什么意思，伯威克医生？」

===Original===

She was trying to remember what it was exactly that Roger Gretorex had said about a “postmortem.” Much that he had said, during that conversation which had meant so little to him, and so much to her, was almost terribly present to her mind. But her memory as to that alarming word or expression had become dim.

Ivy Lexton had always remained, until today, most comfortably ignorant of all the terrible, strange, and awful things that now and again occurred outside her own immediate little circle of people and of interests.

The newspaper reports of a really exciting “society case,” of the kind which amused and intrigued her special set of friends, amused and intrigued her too; though only if there was nothing going on at the time in her own life of infinitely greater moment. As to what is called, often erroneously, “a murder mystery” she had never felt any interest at all.

Her look of innocent inquiry at once effaced from Dr. Berwick’s mind what might have been described as a gossamer suspicion which he had now and again entertained, during the last ten days, with regard to his patient’s wife.

===Chinese===

她竭力回想罗杰·格雷托雷克斯关于「验尸」究竟说过些什么。那场谈话于他不过尔尔，于她却重逾千钧；他说过的许多话，此刻仍近乎可怖地盘踞在她心头。唯独关于那个骇人字眼的记忆，已经模糊了。

直到今天为止，艾薇·莱克斯顿始终舒舒服服地一无所知——对那些时时发生在她自己的小圈子、小利害之外的可怖、离奇、骇人的事，一概不知。

报上那些真正耸动的「上流社会案件」的报道——那种能让她那伙特殊朋友觉得有趣、勾起好奇的案子——也让她觉得有趣、勾起好奇；不过前提是，当时她自己生活里没有紧要得多的大事在发生。至于那种常被叫错名头的「谋杀悬案」，她从来不曾有过半点兴趣。

她那一脸天真无辜的询问神色，顿时从伯威克医生心里抹去了那样一种东西——若要形容，可说是过去十天里他间或对病人太太怀有的一缕蛛丝般的疑心。

===Original===

He did not answer her question at once. Instead he asked her slowly, “I suppose you have some man relative who can see to everything for you? Though I advise that no arrangements be made today.”

“No arrangements?” She looked at him surprised. “Does that mean﻿—” she waited for a moment, then went on, “that poor Jervis’s funeral cannot take place as soon as nurse thought it might?”

“Nurse? What did nurse say?” he asked quickly.

She realised at once that she had made a mistake in mentioning nurse.

===Chinese===

他没有立刻回答她的问题，反而缓缓地问她：「我想，您总有一位男性亲眷，可以替您料理一切吧？不过我劝您，今天先不要作任何安排。」

「不作安排？」她惊讶地望着他。「您是说——」她顿了片刻，又接着说，「可怜的杰维斯的葬礼，不能像护士原先想的那么快办了吗？」

「护士？护士说什么了？」他急急地问。

她立刻明白，自己提起护士是说漏了嘴。

===Original===

Ivy was only clever with regard to those men﻿—they were in the great majority﻿—whom she instinctively knew to be strongly attracted to her lovely self.

“Nurse seemed to think that the funeral could be on Thursday,” she answered in a low voice.

“Nothing can be settled till the postmortem has taken place, Mrs. Lexton. Once the cause of death has been ascertained, the funeral can, of course, take place at once.”

Ivy had moved away while he was speaking, and she was now standing by the writing-table, with her back to the window.

===Chinese===

艾薇的机灵，只用在那样一些男人身上——他们占了男人中的绝大多数——她凭本能就知道，他们已被她这可爱的人儿深深吸引。

「护士好像觉得，葬礼可以定在星期四。」她低声答道。

「验尸之前，什么也定不下来，莱克斯顿太太。死因一旦查明，葬礼自然可以立刻举行。」

他说话的当口，艾薇已走开了，此刻正站在写字台旁，背对着窗户。

===Original===

Slowly, mechanically, she repeated: “The cause of death?”

Though she uttered the four words in her usual voice, there had suddenly swept over her a sensation of intense terror.

“I’m sure that you feel quite as anxious as I do, Mrs. Lexton, to know what can have brought about your husband’s death in so sudden and mysterious a fashion,” said Dr. Berwick earnestly. “I have not concealed from you that to me this case, ever since I took charge of Mr. Lexton, presented more than one puzzling feature.”

Though, unlike his wife, he felt quite sure that the attractive, silly young creature before him had never returned Roger Gretorex’s love, she had certainly been foolishly imprudent. With indulgent contempt he told himself that she was the sort of woman who always likes to have an adoring swain hanging about her.

===Chinese===

她慢慢地、机械地重复道：「死因？」

这四个字她虽是用平常的嗓音说出的，一股强烈的恐怖却已蓦地席卷了她的全身。

「我相信，您和我一样急切地想知道，究竟是什么使您丈夫走得这样突然、这样蹊跷，莱克斯顿太太。」伯威克医生恳切地说，「我没有瞒过您：这个病例，打从我接手莱克斯顿先生起，就不止一处透着费解。」

虽说与妻子不同，他确信眼前这个又迷人又愚蠢的年轻造物从不曾回报过罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的爱情，但她无疑愚蠢得过于轻率。他带着宽容的鄙夷暗想，她是那种女人——身边总喜欢有个痴心的仰慕者转来转去。

===Original===

“I think it more than likely,” he said, getting up, and speaking far more lightly than before, “that nothing untoward will be discovered as a result of the postmortem﻿—which, by the way, simply means an examination. But still, if only for my own satisfaction, and I’m sure that my feelings will be shared by Dr. Lancaster, I should like to be able to put what is the truth, rather than a mere guess, as to the cause of Mr. Lexton’s death on the certificate.”

Again he had uttered those awful words﻿—*the cause of﻿ ﻿… death*.

She forced herself to say, with a look of childish appeal, “I daresay you’ll think it strange, Dr. Berwick, but this is the first time in my life that I’ve ever been even in the same house where﻿—”

She stopped, and he supplied the end of her sentence,

===Chinese===

「我想多半不至于，」他站起身来，口气比先前轻快了许多，「验尸会查出什么不祥的结果——顺便说一句，验尸无非就是做一番检查。不过，哪怕只求自己安心——我相信兰开斯特医生也会有同感——我希望写在那张证明书上的，是莱克斯顿先生死因的实情，而不是姑且一猜。」

他又说出了那几个可怕的字眼——*死……的因由*。

她强自开口，脸上带着孩子气的恳求：「您准会觉得奇怪，伯威克医生，可这是我平生头一回，连待在一栋那样的宅子里都是头一回——那宅子里——」

她收住了口，他替她补全了话尾，

===Original===

“﻿—there has been a death? That is not so strange as you may think, Mrs. Lexton. Some people go through a long life without coming in contact even with serious illness.”

“It’s that which makes it all so dreadful,” and again she melted into tears.

She was telling herself that if they really found out anything as a result of﻿—what was that strange, terrifying term?﻿—the postmortem, then this hard-faced man standing there might make a great difference, perhaps all the difference, to her being, well, worried.

Her look of appeal, her tears, did touch the doctor. He asked himself idly what age this lovely little woman could be? She looked so amazingly young. Not a day over twenty! But she must, of course, be much older than that, for Jervis Lexton had talked on one occasion as if they had been married quite a long time.

===Chinese===

「——死过人？这可不像您想的那么稀奇，莱克斯顿太太。有的人一辈子活到老，连一场重病都没沾过边呢。」

「正因为这样，这一切才这么可怕啊。」说着她又泣不成声了。

她心里在盘算：倘若他们真的从——那个古怪吓人的名堂叫什么来着？——验尸里查出点什么，那么这个板着脸站在跟前的男人，兴许就能让她，嗯，麻烦大不一样，说不定天差地别。

她那恳求的神情、她的眼泪，确乎打动了医生。他漫不经心地暗自揣度，这个可爱的小妇人究竟多大年纪？她看上去年轻得惊人。顶多二十岁，一天不多！可她实际上一定比这大得多，因为杰维斯·莱克斯顿有一回说起话来，仿佛他们已成婚多年了。

===Original===

Ivy felt the wave of kindly feeling, and was reassured.

“I shall be so lonely now,” she said plaintively.

“Have you no woman friend who would come and stay with you, Mrs. Lexton? In any case, you ought to communicate with your husband’s lawyers. I suppose Mr. Lexton’s life was insured?”

“No, Jervis was not insured.”

===Chinese===

艾薇感到这股善意涌来，心下安定了。

「往后我可要多孤单哪。」她哀声说。

「您就没有一位女友能来陪您住些日子吗，莱克斯顿太太？无论如何，您应当去联系您丈夫的律师。我想，莱克斯顿先生保过人寿险吧？」

「没有，杰维斯没有保险。」

===Original===

She looked surprised at the question.

“He quarrelled with his lawyer last year,” she added forlornly. “And our one really great friend, Miles Rushworth, lives at Liverpool, but he is in South Africa just now.”

“You mean the owner of the Rushworth Line?”

“Yes, my husband was in his London office. I’m going to cable to Mr. Rushworth. It’s so dreadful to feel I’ve no one to turn to.”

===Chinese===

这话问得她面露讶色。

「他去年跟他的律师闹翻了。」她凄然又补了一句，「我们唯一真正的至交迈尔斯·拉什沃思，家住在利物浦，可他眼下正在南非。」

「您是说拉什沃思轮船公司（the Rushworth Line）的老板？」

「是的，我丈夫就在他的伦敦办事处做事。我这就打算给拉什沃思先生发电报。觉得自己没有一个可以投靠的人，真是太可怕了。」

===Original===

“I should think Dr. Gretorex might be able to help you?”

He uttered the commonplace words in a tone he tried to make matter-of-fact. Still, he threw her a quick look, and he did become aware that the half-question had disturbed her. Though how much he had disturbed her he was never to know.

She turned to the writing-table, and began piling the papers which lay on it to one side, and then there rose before her inner vision a view of Roger Gretorex’s surgery as it had looked on that evening when she had been surprised, just for a moment, by his old charwoman. She saw again the jar labelled “**Arsenic**” standing on the deal table.

“I’m not quite sure where Dr. Gretorex is just now. Besides, he’s so dreadfully busy.”

===Chinese===

「我想，格雷托雷克斯医生总该能帮上您的忙吧？」

这句寻常话，他说得尽量就事论事。饶是如此，他还是迅速瞟了她一眼，也的确察觉这半个问句搅得她心神不宁了。只是搅得她有多深，他永远无从知晓。

她转身对着写字台，动手把台上的文件摞到一边去。随即，她心眼儿前倏地浮起一幅图景：罗杰·格雷托雷克斯那间诊室，那晚的模样——她曾被他的老女佣撞见，就那么一瞬。她又看见了那只立在松木桌上、贴着「**砒霜**」标签的罐子。

「眼下格雷托雷克斯医生在哪儿，我不大清楚。再说，他忙得要命。」

===Original===

Dr. Berwick reminded himself that the poor little woman had undoubtedly been trying to put an end to Gretorex’s infatuation. Gretorex’s own letter had proved that. So it was decent of her not to send for him just now.

“I must be going, Mrs. Lexton, for I have a great deal of work to get through this morning.”

He took her hand in his, and then he felt startled, for it was icy cold. Poor, pretty little thing! She had evidently had a more serious shock than he had supposed. In her own childish way she must have been really fond of that feckless, yet not unattractive, chap.

True, she had been no more use in the sickroom than an officious, affectionate child would have been! And only this last week he had thought it oddly heartless of her to have been out almost the whole of every day. But he realised, now, that she had never before come in contact with serious illness.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

伯威克医生提醒自己，这个可怜的小妇人无疑一直在想法子了断格雷托雷克斯的痴恋。格雷托雷克斯本人那封信便是明证。所以她眼下不叫人来请他，倒也得体。

「我得走了，莱克斯顿太太，今天上午我还有一大堆事要料理。」

他把她的手握在自己手里，随即吃了一惊——那只手冰凉冰凉。可怜又可怜的漂亮小人儿！她受的打击，显然比他料想的更重。用她自己那种孩子气的方式，她必定是真喜欢过那个没出息、却并非不讨人喜欢的小伙子的。

诚然，她在病室里派不上什么用场，充其量不过像个好管闲事、一片热肠的孩子！就在过去这一星期，他还觉得她天天几乎整日外出，心肠冷得出奇。可如今他明白了，她是从前从未沾染过重病的。……

===Original===

However, there could be no doubt as to her real grief and sense of loss. There were black lines round her long-fringed, violet eyes; marks of tears still stained her roseleaf-tinted cheeks. And﻿—and she was really so lovely that now, when bidding her goodbye, he did hold her hand maybe a thought longer than he need have done.

“Get out of doors all you can,” he said feelingly. “Go and walk in Kensington Gardens now, till lunch time. And don’t worry about anything! As far as will be possible, I promise to save you all trouble and anxiety.”

She gave his strong hand an affectionate squeeze.

“I can’t tell you how grateful I am to you, doctor, I shall never forget how awfully kind you’ve been.”

===Chinese===

不过，她的哀恸与失落是做不得假的。她那双睫毛长长、紫罗兰色的眼睛四周挂着黑晕；泪痕还斑斑地印在她那玫瑰花瓣色的脸颊上。而且——而且她实在这样可爱，以致这会儿道别的时候，他握着她的手，确实比必要的多留了那么一忽儿。

「尽量多到户外去走走。」他恳切地说，「现在就去肯辛顿花园散散步，一直散到午饭时分。什么都别发愁！我答应您，但凡做得到，我一定替您省去一切麻烦和忧虑。」

她亲热地紧握了握他那只有力的手。

「我对您真是感激不尽，医生。您待我这样好、这样好得不得了，我一辈子也忘不了。」

===Original===

Till a few moments ago she had thought Dr. Berwick very unkind, but Ivy Lexton was dowered with so great a power of self-deception that she really did believe what she had just said.

As the doctor was going quickly through the hall, Nurse Bradfield came out of the dining-room.

“You wanted to see me, Dr. Berwick?”

“Did I? Well, I don’t think I need trouble you after all, nurse. Wait a moment, though. I’d just like to have your address. I suppose you are leaving here today?”

===Chinese===

就在片刻之前，她还觉得伯威克医生刻薄得很；可艾薇·莱克斯顿天生赋有那样强大的自欺本领，以致她方才说的那番话，真是信以为真的。

医生正快步穿过门厅时，布拉德菲尔德护士从饭厅里走了出来。

「您要见我，伯威克医生？」

「我要见你吗？嗯，我想到底用不着麻烦你了，护士。不过且慢。我只想留下你的地址。你今天就要离开这儿了吧？」

===Original===

Nurse Bradfield was genuinely surprised. She had felt so sure that the doctor thought but poorly of her; and she on her side had no wish to nurse under him again. Still, one never can tell! She took a card out of her handbag, and handed it to him.

“There’s going to be a postmortem,” he said suddenly, and then he looked at her hard. Had she had no suspicion of anything being wrong?

Her evident astonishment answered his unspoken question even before she said in a surprised tone: “Have you any doubt yourself, doctor, as to the cause of Mr. Lexton’s death?”

He nearly replied: “The greatest doubt! In fact, I don’t feel I can sign the death certificate.”

===Chinese===

布拉德菲尔德护士当真吃了一惊。她原以为医生把她看得很低；而她这方面，也无意再在他手底下当护理。不过，世事难料！她从手提包里取出一张名片，递给了他。

「要作一次验尸。」他猝然说，随即紧紧盯住她。对事情有异，她竟没有起过半点疑心吗？

她那显而易见的错愕，没等她开口便已回答了他未曾出口的疑问；随后她才用惊讶的语气说：「医生，您自己对莱克斯顿先生的死因，也有怀疑吗？」

他险些答出口：「怀疑极了！说实话，我觉得这张死亡证明我签不了。」

===Original===

But he checked himself. It wouldn’t do for her to go and frighten that poor little woman. After all, his suspicions might be﻿—he certainly hoped they were﻿—absolutely unjustified.

And then it was her turn to astonish him.

“I hope Mrs. Lexton won’t be put to any great expense,” she murmured. “In spite of this lovely flat, and her wonderful clothes, I’m afraid they were very poor. In fact, Mr. Lexton, when he was wandering so much these last two days, talked a lot about money, and seemed to blame himself very much. But I should say that it was she who was extravagant!”

“Extravagant?” said the doctor, surprised. “Is Mrs. Lexton extravagant? I should have thought she had very simple tastes.”

===Chinese===

可他把话忍住了。可不能让她跑去吓着那个可怜的小妇人。毕竟，他的疑心也许——他自然巴望如此——全然是无稽的。

接着，反倒轮到她让他吃惊了。

「但愿莱克斯顿太太别为这个破费太多。」她低声道，「别看这套公寓这么漂亮、她的衣裳那么讲究，我怕他们穷得很呢。说实在的，莱克斯顿先生这最后两天神志昏沉，翻来覆去说了许多钱的事，好像很怪自己。可要叫我说，挥霍的正是她！」

「挥霍？」医生诧异道，「莱克斯顿太太挥霍吗？我还当她的趣味挺简朴呢。」

===Original===

Nurse Bradfield smiled to herself. “Men are soft where a pretty face is concerned,” she reminded herself tolerantly.

Then aloud she said: “Mrs. Lexton spends a great deal of money over her clothes﻿—and I know that she is a good bit in debt. There was a man here the day before yesterday who said he wouldn’t go away till he was paid. But he had to, at last, for she wasn’t in till midnight. We must hope that Mr. Lexton was well insured.”

“He wasn’t insured at all,” said the doctor shortly. “I asked Mrs. Lexton, for had he been, she ought at once to have informed the insurance company.”

===Chinese===

布拉德菲尔德护士暗自一笑。「一碰到漂亮脸蛋儿，男人就软了。」她宽厚地提醒自己。

随后她出声说道：「莱克斯顿太太在衣裳上花的钱可不少——我知道她还欠着好一笔债呢。前天这儿来过一个男人，说不拿到钱就不走。可到头来他还是只好走了，因为她直到半夜才回家。我们只好指望莱克斯顿先生保险保得足了。」

「他压根儿没保险。」医生简短地说，「我问过莱克斯顿太太了，倘若保过险，她本该立刻通知保险公司的。」

---

===Original===

For the first time in his professional life Dr. Berwick went home in what he himself described as “the middle of his round.”

Mrs. Berwick saw the motor draw up outside their little house, and running out into the hall she opened the front door.

“Darling!” she cried, “did you forget anything?”

“No, Janey, I forgot nothing. But I’ve got to arrange for a postmortem, so I thought it better to come back here, rather than ring up from a call office.”

===Chinese===

行医生涯里头一遭，伯威克医生在照他自己说法「巡诊中途」的当口回了家。

伯威克太太瞧见汽车在他们那所小宅子外停了下来，便奔进门厅，拉开了前门。

「亲爱的！」她喊道，「你落什么东西了吗？」

「没有，杰妮，我什么都没落。可我得安排一次验尸，所以我想还是回趟家好，省得去电话亭里打。」

===Original===

She saw that he was excited and disturbed, and, being a wise woman, she asked him no questions. But she was not surprised when, instead of going straight off to the telephone, he turned into their sitting-room, and shut the door behind him.

“Janey?” he said slowly. “You know that that poor chap Lexton died last night? Mrs. Lexton, as usual, was out. She came in to find him dead.”

“How dreadful!”

“He was better, it seems, in the morning﻿—very much better. Then Roger Gretorex came in and sat with him some time alone or so I gather from the nurse.”

===Chinese===

她看得出他心里激动、烦乱；她是个明白人，便一句也没问。他没有径直去打电话，而是拐进了他们的起居室，随手带上了门——对此她并不意外。

「杰妮？」他缓缓地说，「你知道那个可怜的家伙莱克斯顿昨夜死了吗？莱克斯顿太太呢，照旧在外面。她回到家，发现他已经死了。」

「多可怕呀！」

「早上他好像见好了——好多了。随后罗杰·格雷托雷克斯来了，单独陪他坐了一阵子——至少我是从护士那儿听来的。」

===Original===

There followed a long, pregnant silence between the husband and wife. Then there came over Mrs. Berwick’s face a look of terrible dismay.

“D’you mean that you suspect﻿—?” And there was a world of horror in her voice.

“I don’t suspect anything,” he answered sharply. “And I certainly don’t want you to put words into my mouth, or even thoughts into my mind. There’s going to be a postmortem, so we shall soon know part of the truth, at any rate.”

She waited a moment, and her voice sank almost to a whisper.

===Chinese===

夫妻之间继而是长长一段意味深长的沉默。随即，伯威克太太脸上掠过一阵骇然的惊惶。

「你是说，你疑心——？」她的嗓音里透着无穷的恐怖。

「我什么也不疑心。」他厉声答道，「我也决计不要你往我嘴里塞话，甚至往我心里塞念头。反正要验尸了，不管怎样，我们很快就会知道一部分真相。」

她等了片刻，声音低得几乎成了耳语。

===Original===

“Then Mrs. Lexton consents to a postmortem?”

“Her consent was not asked,” he said brusquely. “But I’ll tell you one thing, Janey. If there’s been any foul play, she’s not in it. I’ve thoroughly satisfied myself of that.”

“But Roger Gretorex? A doctor? How terrible!”

“I know,” he muttered. “But, Janey?”

===Chinese===

「那么，是莱克斯顿太太同意验尸的？」

「没有征求过她的同意。」他生硬地说，「不过有一件事我可以告诉你，杰妮。倘若有人做了手脚，那里面也没有她的份。这一点我已经彻底查明白了。」

「可罗杰·格雷托雷克斯呢？一个医生？多可怕呀！」

「我知道。」他喃喃道，「不过，杰妮？」

===Original===

“Yes, dear?”

“I think it’s ninety-nine chances to one against my half-suspicion turning out to be the truth. What with his impudence in prescribing for my patient, and that queer love-letter of his﻿—well, I’m prejudiced against the chap.”

“I’m not surprised at that,” she breathed.

Indeed, she, Janey Berwick herself, felt strongly prejudiced against Roger Gretorex.

===Chinese===

「嗯，亲爱的？」

「我想，我这半截子疑心成真的机会，是一比九十九。又是他放肆给我的病人开方子，又是他那封古怪的情书——唉，我对这家伙存了偏见。」

「这我一点也不奇怪。」她低声说。

确实，她，杰妮·伯威克自己，也对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯怀着强烈的偏见。

## X / 十

===Original===

As Inspector Orpington, of the Criminal Investigation Department, Scotland Yard, entered dusty, poverty-stricken Ferry Place, he made up his mind that he would be, so far as was possible in the circumstances, frank with the man he was on his way to see with regard to Jervis Lexton’s death.

Like many an Englishman of his type, he had his own clear, if unexpressed, philosophy of life. He preferred the straight to the tortuous way, and that, it may incidentally be observed, is true of all the really successful men in his peculiar line of work.

Such men naturally suffer from the defects of their qualities. Inspector Orpington had no belief in what he called to himself the French methods of criminal investigation. For one thing, he was convinced, and backed his conviction from experience, that in the vast majority of cases there is seldom anything mysterious, or out of the way, even in the best-planned and most intelligent type of crime.

With regard to the case concerning which he had just been ordered to make certain preliminary inquiries, the story he had been able to piece together was even now, from his point of view, a straightforward story of illicit love leading to a cold-blooded and cruel murder. He had already interviewed the dead man’s two regular medical attendants, his trained nurse, and last, but by no means least, his tearful, hysterical, and singularly attractive young widow.

===Chinese===

苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）刑事调查部（Criminal Investigation Department）的奥平顿探长（Inspector Orpington），踏进尘土飞扬、穷困寒酸的费里街时，心中已打定主意：关于杰维斯·莱克斯顿的死，只消眼下情势许可，他就要对即将登门求见的那个人开诚布公。

和许多他这一类的英国人一样，他自有一套明白晓畅却从不宣之于口的人生哲学。他宁走直路，不走曲径——顺便说一句，凡在他这门特殊行当里真正成事的人物，莫不如此。

这种人自然也免不了优点带来的缺憾。奥平顿探长对他私下唤作“法兰西式刑侦法”的那一套，全然不信。一来，他有一个由阅历撑腰的信念：绝大多数案子里，即便是最周密、最高明的犯罪，也极少有什么神秘难解或异乎寻常之处。

至于他刚刚奉命着手初步查访的这个案子，在他看来，至今拼凑出的故事仍旧是一个直截了当的故事：一段私情，酿成了一场冷血而残忍的谋杀。死者的两位常年主治医生、他的专业护士，以及最后一位——却也绝非最不打紧的一位——那位泪眼汪汪、歇斯底里却又格外动人的年轻遗孀，他都已一一问过了。

===Original===

Ivy Lexton remained, in Inspector Orpington’s mind, the one point of doubt and mystery, if indeed “mystery” you could call it, in the affair.

He had found it very difficult to make up his mind as to whether Mrs. Lexton was entirely innocent regarding the events which had led to Lexton’s death. He had detected certain flaws in the story she had appeared, in spite of her agitation, so willing to tell. But he had been naturally impressed by the firm conviction expressed by the two doctors who had attended Jervis Lexton. They had both declared that their patient’s wife had been not only innocent, but quite unsuspicious, of the sinister tragedy which had undoubtedly been enacted during the fortnight which had preceded her husband’s death.

Dr. Berwick had said brusquely: “Why, the woman was never there! She was out morning, noon, and night. I myself only saw her three times, each time only for a few minutes, and only once with her husband.”

===Chinese===

在奥平顿探长心里，艾薇·莱克斯顿始终是这桩案子里唯一疑云未开、暧昧难明的一点——倘若这也当得起“神秘”二字的话。

莱克斯顿太太对于酿成莱克斯顿之死的种种情事是否全然无辜，他一直觉得极难裁断。在她不顾激动、看似急欲倾诉的那番叙述里，他察觉出几处破绽。但两位主治杰维斯·莱克斯顿的医生都异口同声地深信不疑，他自然不能无所触动。两人都宣称：病人的妻子非但无辜，而且对她丈夫死前那两个星期里无疑上演过的阴森悲剧，始终毫无觉察。

伯威克医生粗声粗气地说：“唉，那女人压根儿就不在家！早、午、晚都不见人影。我自己统共只见过她三回，回回不过几分钟光景，而且只有一回是同她丈夫在一起的。”

===Original===

Orpington had also been struck by the liking both the nurse and the cook showed for Lexton’s widow; and that though they both admitted she was selfish and pleasure-loving.

But what weighed the scale most of all in favour of Ivy’s complete innocence was the fact that the life of Jervis Lexton had not been insured, and that with her husband apparently disappeared the poor, pretty little woman’s only source of income.

Long experience had convinced Inspector Orpington that there are only two outstanding motives for secret murder﻿—money, and the passion called love. As he considered the story in all its bearings, it seemed plain to him that, whereas it had been very much to Ivy Lexton’s interest to keep her husband alive, this young man, Roger Gretorex, had undoubtedly had a strong motive for compassing his death. Mrs. Lexton, in the course of the long examination and cross-examination to which she had been submitted this morning, had admitted, albeit with a certain reluctance, that Roger Gretorex not only passionately loved her, but had also at various times pestered her with unwelcome attentions.

===Chinese===

护士和厨娘都对莱克斯顿的遗孀颇有好感，这也叫奥平顿看在眼里；何况她们俩都承认，她自私、贪图享乐，那点好感却依旧不减。

然而，最叫天平彻底倒向艾薇全然无辜一边的，是这样一个事实：杰维斯·莱克斯顿生前并未投保寿险，而随着丈夫的死去，这位可怜的、娇俏的小妇人显然连唯一的生活来源也一并消失了。

长期的阅历使奥平顿探长确信：暗中杀人，动机不出两个——钱财，与唤作爱情的情欲。他把这故事里里外外掂量一遍，便觉得一目了然：保全丈夫的性命，于艾薇·莱克斯顿的利益攸关至大；而这个年轻人罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，却无疑怀有谋害他性命的强烈动机。今天上午，莱克斯顿太太经过长时间的盘问与反盘问，已经招认——虽说不无勉强——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯不仅狂热地爱着她，还不止一次纠缠不休，向她献过她并不领情的殷勤。

---

===Original===

Everyone interested in the detection of crime is aware that persons arrested on a charge of murder are always solemnly warned that anything said by them may be used in evidence against them. But probably few people know that the giving of any such warning is left to the discretion of the C.I.D. men who are engaged in those preliminary inquiries which, in the majority of cases, do end by bringing a murderer to justice. Certain rules are laid down for their guidance; but even so a great deal is left to the ordering of their own consciences, to what each individual considers it fair or unfair to ask of one who may be actually suspected of having committed the crime under investigation.

Orpington’s object was to get at the truth. It was his considered opinion that the guilty are painfully alive to their danger, and will go to any length to protect themselves. In their case the plainest warning is wasted. As to an innocent witness, he believed the best way to put him or her at ease is to be reasonably frank.

He felt sure that, in Roger Gretorex, he would find one both forewarned and forearmed. What he desired to know, as a result of his coming interview with the young doctor, was how far Mrs. Lexton had told the truth as to her relations with the man who, owing to his passion for herself, had almost certainly poisoned her husband.

===Chinese===

凡是关注罪案侦破的人都知道，因谋杀罪名被捕的人总会被郑重其事地告诫：他所说的一切，都可能被用作对他不利的证据。但恐怕很少有人知道，这类告诫给或不给、几时给，全凭从事初步查访的刑事调查部人员的斟酌——而这类查访，在多数案子里的结局，正是将杀人犯绳之以法。上头固然为他们订了几条守则以资遵循；但即便如此，许多分寸仍要交由各人的良心来裁夺：对于某个实际上已涉嫌所查罪行的人，什么问得、什么问不得，各人自有各人的公道。

奥平顿的目的在于查个水落石出。依他深思熟虑的见解，有罪之人对自身的危境最是敏感不过，为保全自己无所不用其极——对这种人，把警告说得再明白也是白费。至于无辜的证人，他相信，叫他们安下心来的最好法子，就是通情达理地以诚相待。

他确信，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯必是个已有警觉、且有防备的人。而他想从与这位年轻医生的这场会面里探明的，是莱克斯顿太太在交代她与那个男人的关系时，究竟说了几分真话——正是这个男人，因了对她自己的炽烈情欲，几乎可以断定毒死了她的丈夫。

===Original===

Slowly he walked, with the sergeant he had brought with him, down the now deserted, airless little street. What a contrast to the broad avenue in which stood the fine block of flats known as Duke of Kent Mansion! It was difficult to believe that the woman with whom he had spent over an hour this morning, in the shadowed drawing-room where everything spelt not only comfort, but affluence and luxury, could have been on terms of close intimacy with a man who lived in Ferry Place.

Before he had time to ring the bell, the narrow front door of the tiny two-storied house opened, and the person he had come to see and, he felt sure, to convict of the most hideous and cruel form of murder known to civilised man, stood before him.

What a fine young chap! And what a haughty, sombre, defiant countenance!

===Chinese===

他带着手下的警士，缓步走在这条此刻空无一人、密不透风的小街上。同那条宽阔的林荫大道相比，真是天壤之别——那座名叫肯特公爵大厦的漂亮公寓楼，便矗立在大道旁。今天上午，他在那间背阴的客厅里同一位妇人周旋了一个多钟头，那里的一切不仅透着舒适，更透着富足与奢华；要说这样一位妇人，竟同一个住在费里街的男人过从甚密，实在叫人难以相信。

他还没来得及按铃，这座两层小宅那扇狭窄的前门便开了，他要找的那个人站在了他面前——他并且确信，此人终将被定罪，罪名是文明人类所知的最阴森、最残忍的一种谋杀。

多好的一个小伙子！却又是一副多么倨傲、阴沉、桀骜不驯的面孔！

===Original===

Yet the voice in which Gretorex uttered the commonplace words, “Will you come in?” was a deep, pleasant, cultivated voice.

The doctor led his two visitors into his consulting-room, a room which, though poorly furnished, was yet, as the inspector quickly noted, that of a man accustomed to the amenities of life. For one thing he noticed the lovely bunch of fragrant hothouse flowers standing in a glass on the writing-table. He felt what he very seldom did feel﻿—surprised.

As the three men sat down, Gretorex full in what light came in through his one window, the inspector observed:

===Chinese===

然而格雷托雷克斯说出“您请进”这句寻常客套话时，那嗓音却低沉、悦耳，一听便知是受过教养的。

医生把两位来客引进自己的诊室。这间屋子陈设虽然简陋，但正如探长一眼便注意到的，主人是个素来享用惯了生活雅趣的人。单说写字台上那只玻璃瓶里插着的一大捧温房培育的香花，便足以为证。他生出了平日罕有的感觉——诧异。

三人落座，格雷托雷克斯正对着那扇独窗透进来的光，探长开口道：

===Original===

“My name, doctor, as I told you over the telephone, is Orpington, and I am attached to the Criminal Investigation Department of Scotland Yard. I have come to ask you certain questions concerning the death of a gentleman who was at one time, I understand, a patient of yours.”

“If you will tell me his name, I will look up his case,” said Gretorex quickly.

“His name was Jervis Lexton, and his death took place last Tuesday in Flat 9 of Duke of Kent Mansion, Kensington.”

“Jervis Lexton was never a patient of mine,” the young man answered firmly; and then he hesitated, and finally added, “He was a friend﻿—I suppose I might even say a great friend.”

===Chinese===

“医生，我在电话里已经对您说过，敝姓奥平顿，供职于苏格兰场刑事调查部。我来，是要就一位先生的死向您问几个问题——据我了解，这位先生从前是您的病人。”

“您若肯告诉我他的姓名，我便去调他的病历。”格雷托雷克斯很快地答道。

“他名叫杰维斯·莱克斯顿，已于上星期二死在肯辛顿肯特公爵大厦的9号套间。”

“杰维斯·莱克斯顿从来不是我的病人。”年轻人斩钉截铁地答道；随即他迟疑了一下，终于又添上一句：“他是一位朋友——我想，甚至可以说是一位挚友。”

===Original===

“Are you already aware of the circumstances concerning Mr. Jervis Lexton’s death, Dr. Gretorex?”

And then Roger Gretorex told the first of the lies he felt it incumbent on him to tell during this, to him, terrible interview.

“I’ve been in the country for nearly a week, and I only learnt on my return here, this morning, of Mr. Lexton’s death. I am as yet unaware of the circumstances to which I presume I owe your visit.”

===Chinese===

“关于杰维斯·莱克斯顿先生死时的情形，您已经知晓了吗，格雷托雷克斯医生？”

于是，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯说出了他觉得在这场于他而言可怖至极的问话中义不容辞要说的第一句谎话。

“我在乡下待了将近一个星期，今早回到这里，才得知莱克斯顿先生的死讯。至于我料想招致您此番光临的那些情形，我至今一无所知。”

===Original===

He waited a moment, then told the inspector what was indeed the truth:

“I was exceedingly surprised to learn of his death, for I had seen him just before leaving town. Though I thought him far less well than the last time I had been with him, there was nothing to indicate the seriousness of his condition.”

===Chinese===

他顿了一顿，然后对探长说了一句确确实实的真话：

“听到他的死讯，我惊愕不已，因为离城之前我还见过他。虽然我觉得他大不如上次见面时健旺，却全看不出他的病情有丝毫凶险的迹象。”

===Original===

“Yet you told the nurse that you were dismayed by the charge in his appearance?”

“I daresay I did. She thought him distinctly better, I do remember that, and I disagreed with her.”

“You did not ask to see Mr. Lexton, Dr. Gretorex. The nurse tells me your call was on Mrs. Lexton. As that lady was out, Nurse Bradfield, I understand, suggested you should see her patient, as she thought it would cheer him up.”

“That is so, and I was not with him for more than ten minutes.”

===Chinese===

“可您却对护士说过，他容貌的变化令您心惊？”

“我多半是说过。她觉得他分明见好——这个我记得——而我不以为然。”

“您并没有求见莱克斯顿先生，格雷托雷克斯医生。护士告诉我，您那回来访，访的是莱克斯顿太太。那位太太外出不在，据我了解，是布拉德菲尔德护士提议您去看看她的病人，她想着这能让他高兴高兴。”

“确是如此，我陪他统共没待过十分钟。”

===Original===

“You were, I think, alone with him during that time?”

“Yes, I was.”

“You went down to the country immediately after seeing him?”

“Yes. I went to my home in the country the same afternoon, and, as I told you just now, I only came back this morning.”

===Chinese===

“那段时间里，就您同他两个人在一起，是吗？”

“是的。”

“见过他之后，您当即就下乡去了？”

“是的。当天下午我就回了乡间的家，而且方才已对您说过，我是今早才回来的。”

===Original===

“Mr. Jervis Lexton died during the evening of the day you saw him﻿—that is, on Tuesday, the 16th of November. His regular medical attendant, Dr. Berwick, was not satisfied as to the cause of death. A postmortem was held on the Thursday, and revealed the fact that Lexton’s death was due to a large dose of arsenic administered some hours before death. According to Nurse Bradfield, you, Dr. Gretorex, were the last person, apart from herself and, I believe, the cook, who saw him alive. That is why I am here.”

Gretorex stared at the speaker in silence; and, gradually, all the colour ebbed from his face.

In spite of himself the inspector felt sorry for the young man. He told himself that Roger Gretorex evidently saw the game was up. Still, the doctor looked the sort of chap who would put up a fight for it.

===Chinese===

“杰维斯·莱克斯顿先生死在您见他的那天傍晚——也就是十一月十六日，星期二。他的常年主治医生伯威克医生，对死因不能满意。星期四做了验尸，查明莱克斯顿之死，是死前数小时服下的大剂量砒霜所致。据布拉德菲尔德护士说，除了她自己，还有——我记得是——厨娘，您格雷托雷克斯医生，便是最后一个见到他活着的人。我此来，正为此事。”

格雷托雷克斯默默盯着说话的人；血色渐渐地从他脸上褪尽。

探长不由得对这年轻人起了怜意。他暗自思忖，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯显然明白大势已去。不过，这位医生看样子是那种还要负隅顽抗一下的角色。

===Original===

Inspector Orpington made an almost imperceptible sign to the sergeant he had brought with him, and the man at once quietly left the room.

Orpington got up and looked out of the window until he saw his sergeant in the street outside. Then he turned and said to Gretorex:

“I sent my sergeant out of the room, doctor, because I am obliged now to ask you a question which I thought you would prefer to have put to you privately. You were, I understand, a friend of Mrs. Lexton’s as well as a friend of her husband?”

===Chinese===

奥平顿探长朝他带来的警士递了个几乎察觉不出的眼色，那人立刻悄悄退出了房间。

奥平顿站起身，望着窗外，直到瞧见他的警士已到了街上。他这才回过头来，对格雷托雷克斯说：

“医生，我之所以让警士退出房去，是因为眼下不得不问您一个问题——我想，您会愿意这话只私下里对您一个人讲。据我了解，您既是她丈夫的朋友，也是莱克斯顿太太本人的朋友，对吗？”

===Original===

“I was on terms of friendship with them both,” and his face turned deeply red.

“But you saw much more of Mrs. Lexton than you did of her husband?”

This was a bow drawn at a venture, and it brought down the quarry.

===Chinese===

“我同他们两位都有交情。”说着，他的脸涨得通红。

“可您同莱克斯顿太太的来往，远比同她丈夫的多得多，是吧？”

这一箭原是信手射出的，却正中猎物。

===Original===

“I sometimes escorted Mrs. Lexton to a picture gallery, and now and again we went to a theatre together. But﻿—” he waited a moment, and the colour ebbed from his face. Though what he was going to say was true, he hated saying it﻿—“Mr. and Mrs. Lexton always seemed on the best of terms together.”

“So I understand. But I am not seeking information as to the relations of Mr. and Mrs. Lexton. What I wish to suggest, without offence, is that you, Dr. Gretorex, would have liked to have been on closer terms of friendship with Mrs. Lexton than she thought it right to allow? I will be frank with you﻿—Mrs. Lexton has admitted as much.”

A burning flush again rose to Gretorex’s dark face. Poor Ivy! Poor, foolish little darling! He did not feel the slightest feeling of anger with her. He only felt a choking sensation of dismay. Whatever had possessed her to say such a thing?

===Chinese===

“我有时陪莱克斯顿太太去看看画展，偶尔也一同去看场戏。不过——”他顿了片刻，血色从脸上褪了下去。他要说的话虽然属实，说出来却叫他痛恨——“莱克斯顿先生和太太两人在一起，向来显得情深意笃。”

“这个我也听说了。只是我要打听的，并非莱克斯顿先生与太太之间的夫妻关系。我冒昧想点破的是：格雷托雷克斯医生，您原本巴望同莱克斯顿太太结下的交情，比她认为得体的分寸还要亲近，对吗？我不妨对您直说——莱克斯顿太太已亲口承认了这一点。”

一阵滚烫的红潮又涌上格雷托雷克斯黝黑的脸。可怜的艾薇！可怜又痴傻的小心肝！他对她没有生出丝毫怨怼，只觉得一阵郁闷哽在喉头，透不过气来。她到底是着了什么魔，竟说出这样的话来？

===Original===

He answered, speaking quietly, passionlessly, “Mrs. Lexton is a very attractive woman, and a beautiful woman. It is difficult to be with her without feeling inclined to﻿—well﻿—” and as he hesitated, the older man smiled.

“To make love to her? I absolutely agree, Dr. Gretorex. Though she was naturally very much upset when I saw her this morning, I thought Mrs. Lexton one of the most engaging, as well as one of the best-looking, young ladies I had ever come across.”

Poor Gretorex! He would have liked to have struck Inspector Orpington across the face, and yet his own words had called up the look that had so grossly offended him on the other’s countenance, and had also provoked his remark.

“Do you admit, Dr. Gretorex, that you were very much attracted to this lady?”

===Chinese===

他答话了，语气平静，不带一丝火气：“莱克斯顿太太是个极有魅力的女人，也是个美丽的女人。同她相处，很难不生出想要——呃——”他迟疑着，年长的那位微微一笑。

“想要向她求爱？我完全同意，格雷托雷克斯医生。今早见到她时，她固然心烦意乱得很，可我还是觉得，莱克斯顿太太算得上我平生所见最妩媚动人、也最标致的年轻女子之一。”

可怜的格雷托雷克斯！他恨不得一记耳光打在奥平顿探长脸上；然而，正是他自己方才那句话，引出了对方脸上那副叫他如此不堪忍受的神情，也招来了对方那句评语。

“格雷托雷克斯医生，您承认自己曾对这位太太大为倾心吗？”

===Original===

“You put me in a difficult position; but I admit that perhaps I did say one or two foolish things to her.”

He was wondering, with a feeling of agonising anxiety, whether Ivy had kept his letters.

“Did Mrs. Lexton ever by chance come here, to 6 Ferry Place?”

“She came to tea on one occasion, but not alone, of course. A friend of hers, a widow called Mrs. Arundell, and a man friend of Mrs. Arundell’s, came with her.”

===Chinese===

“您这可把我置于难堪的境地了；不过我承认，我或许确实对她说过一两句糊涂话。”

他心里正怀着一种煎熬般的焦虑，暗自揣度着：艾薇到底留没留着他的那些信。

“莱克斯顿太太可曾碰巧来过这儿，来过费里街6号？”

“她来喝过一回茶，不过当然不是独自来的。同她一道来的，有她的一位朋友——一位寡居的太太，唤作阿伦德尔太太——还有阿伦德尔太太的一位男性朋友。”

===Original===

And then Roger Gretorex leant forward:

“I do hope that you will believe me when I tell you that any﻿—well, feeling of attraction, was entirely on my side. When I did, I admit very foolishly, once try to tell her﻿—”

He stopped, and the other interjected, not unkindly, “How much she attracted you?”

Gretorex nodded, and then he gasped out the lying words﻿—“She made me feel at once she was not that kind of woman.”

“I suppose,” said the inspector with a twinkle in his eyes, “that Mrs. Lexton used that very expression.”

===Chinese===

接着，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯把身子往前一探：

“我恳切希望您能相信我的话：所谓——呃——倾慕之情，完完全全是在我这一边。我承认自己愚蠢透顶，曾有那么一次试图向她挑明——”

他顿住了，对方接了话头，语气并不刻薄：“被她迷到了什么程度？”

格雷托雷克斯点了点头，随即喘着气吐出那句谎话——“她让我当下便明白，她不是那种女人。”

“我料想，”探长眼里闪过一丝狡黠，“莱克斯顿太太用的正是这个说法。”

===Original===

Gretorex tried to smile back.

“Well, yes, I believe she did. It happened a long time ago, in fact when I first made the Lextons’ acquaintance.”

Now this observation gave the direct lie to Ivy Lexton’s statement, which Orpington honestly believed had been extracted from her against her will.

“I suppose that you can suggest no reason why this man, Jervis Lexton, should have wished to take his own life?”

“No, none at all. He had just obtained an excellent job.”

===Chinese===

格雷托雷克斯想强作笑容回应。

“呃，是的，我相信她的确是这么说的。那是很久以前的事了，其实正是我初次结识莱克斯顿夫妇的时候。”

这句话，一下就证明艾薇·莱克斯顿的供词是彻头彻尾的谎话；而奥平顿由衷相信，那份供词本是违背她的本意逼出来的。

“我想，您也说不出半点缘由，为什么这个人——杰维斯·莱克斯顿——竟会想要自寻短见吧？”

“是的，半点也说不出。他刚刚谋得一份极好的差事。”

===Original===

“You can throw no light either, I presume, as to how the arsenic which undoubtedly caused his death can have been administered to him?”

“Not only can I throw no light on it, but I find it almost impossible to believe what my reason tells me is true﻿—your assertion that his death was directly due to the administration of arsenic.”

The speaker’s voice was strong, assured. At last he was on firm ground.

“I take it there is a surgery attached to this house, and that you make up your own medicines?”

The inspector asked that vital question in a very quiet tone, but Gretorex realised its purport as he answered, “I do﻿—for the most part.”

===Chinese===

“至于那确凿无疑致他死命的砒霜，究竟是怎么施到他身上去的，我想您同样提不出什么线索吧？”

“我非但提不出线索，而且简直无法相信理智告诉我确属事实的那句话——您断言他的死，直接就是砒霜所致。”

说这话时，他的嗓音有力而笃定。他总算站到了坚实的地面上。

“据我所知，这宅子附设着一间诊室，而且药剂都是您亲手配制的吧？”

这个要害问题，探长问得声调极轻；可格雷托雷克斯一面作答，一面就咂出了其中的分量：“是的——多半是自己配的。”

===Original===

“I should like to see the surgery.”

“By all means.”

Roger Gretorex got up. Then he placed his back against the door.

Instantly Inspector Orpington, though he was a brave man, and had been in more than one very tight corner, felt a cold tremor run through him. Was this fine-looking young chap going to whip out a revolver and kill, not only himself, but also the man whose unpleasant duty it had been to show him that the game he had been so mad as to play was up?

But he need not have been afraid.

“Look here! Before I take you into my surgery, where you will find a jar of arsenic as likely as not on an open shelf﻿—for I am a careless chap, and no one has access to the place but myself and my old charwoman﻿—I want to say something to you. I don’t suppose you will believe me, but I wish to tell you, here and now, that I have no more idea of how poor Lexton got at the arsenic which caused his death﻿—if it did cause it﻿—than you have, and that the one thing of which I am quite sure is that it did not come out of my surgery.”

===Chinese===

“我想看看那间诊室。”

“请便。”

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯站起身来。接着，他把背抵在了门上。

刹那间，奥平顿探长只觉一阵寒颤窜遍全身——他虽是个胆气十足的人，也不止一次身陷过九死一生的绝境。这个仪表堂堂的年轻人，莫非要掏出左轮手枪，把两条性命一并结果——不光是他自己，还有那个人：正是那人担着这桩不讨好的差事，来向他挑明，他疯狂到这般地步玩下的把戏，已经到头了？

但他的担心是多余的。

“听我说！趁我还没领您进我的诊室——在那儿，您十有八九会在敞开的架子上看到一罐砒霜，因为我是个粗心大意的人，除了我和我那个打杂的老女佣，谁也进不得那地方——我有话要对您讲。我想您多半不会相信，但我愿意就在此时此地告诉您：可怜的莱克斯顿是怎么弄到那害他丧命的砒霜的——倘若那真是死因的话——我心中知晓的分毫不多过您；而我唯一能断言的是，那东西绝非出自我的诊室。”

## XI / 十一

===Original===

Instead of doing what he ought to have done﻿—that is, to have sought at once the best legal aid in his power﻿—Roger Gretorex made up his mind to go back to Sussex, if only for a few hours.

Ivy’s words of agonised fear now found an echo in his own heart. His mother must hear the very few and simple facts concerning Jervis Lexton’s death from himself.

On his way to the station he saw two newspaper placards, and he felt as if it was at him that they shouted the ominous words:

> Kensington 

 Poisoning 

 Mystery.

> Well-Known 

 Clubman 

 Poisoned.

===Chinese===

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯没有去做他本该做的事——也就是说，立刻去寻访他力所能及的最好法律援手——而是打定了主意回一趟萨塞克斯，哪怕只待几个钟头。

艾薇那番痛切的惊惧之词，如今在他自己心里得到了回响。关于杰维斯·莱克斯顿之死，那寥寥几件简单的事实，必须由他亲口讲给母亲听。

去车站的路上，他看见两张报馆招贴，只觉得那一个个不祥的字眼，正冲着自己叫喊：

> 肯辛顿

 毒杀

 疑云。

> 知名

 俱乐部会员

 遭毒杀。

===Original===

He bought an evening paper in the station, and then, when he unfolded it, he felt a sharp stab of anger and disgust. In the centre of the front page was a charming portrait of Ivy﻿—Ivy looking her sweetest and most seductive self. Above and below the photograph was printed a series of paragraphs dealing with the joyous life the young couple had led in the carefree existence which centres round the idler members of the fashionable nightclubs. It was also stated that, on the very night of Mr. Lexton’s unexpected death, Mrs. Lexton was supping at the Savoy with “a smart theatre-party.”

In the grateful darkness of a late November afternoon, Roger Gretorex walked the two miles which separated the little station from Anchorford, the village which he still felt part of the very warp and woof of his life, though he owned practically no land there. All that his father had been able to keep was the manor house, and the little portion of the park which had surrounded the dwelling-house of the owners of Anchorford from the days of Domesday Book.

Now and again Gretorex, as he hurried through the narrow lanes, would tell himself that the inexplicable mystery attaching to Jervis Lexton’s death by poison was bound to be cleared up, and probably in some quite simple way﻿—a way that he himself was now too excited and too anxious to think out for himself.

Then there would come a sudden sensation of doubt, of despondency. Like Ivy, but with far more cause, Roger Gretorex began to feel as if a net were closing round him.
