At last he turned into the long avenue which led to Anchorford House, and his heart leapt when he saw the long Elizabethan front, now bright with twinkling lights.

He rang the front door bell, and then he schooled himself to wait patiently for old Bolton, who, once his father’s head groom, now acted as general factotum and odd-job man.

===Chinese===

他在车站里买了一份晚报，展开一看，心头顿觉一阵气恼与厌恶的锐刺。头版正中印着艾薇的一幅动人肖像——照片上的艾薇，正是她最甜美、最勾人心魄的模样。照片上下排着一段段文字，讲这对小夫妇以往那种快活日子——以时髦夜总会的闲散人群为轴心的无忧无虑的生活。报上还写道，就在莱克斯顿先生猝然死去的那天夜里，莱克斯顿太太还正同「一场时髦的看戏聚会」在萨伏伊饭店吃夜宵。

十一月末的下午，天色善意地黑了下来；罗杰·格雷托雷克斯从小站步行两英里，走到安克福德村。他在那里虽已几乎不名一寸土地，却总觉得这村子仍是他生命经纬的一部分。他父亲能守住的家业，只剩庄园的宅邸，和环抱着宅邸的一小片园子——打从《末日审判书》（Domesday Book）的年代起，安克福德历代主人的居所便坐落在这片园子的中央。

格雷托雷克斯匆匆穿过窄巷时，时不时告诉自己：缠绕着杰维斯·莱克斯顿中毒之死的这道难解之谜，终究会水落石出，而且多半会用某种再简单不过的方式了结——只是那种方式，他此刻太亢奋、太焦灼，自己想不出来罢了。

可随即又会涌上一阵疑惧、一阵沮丧。同艾薇一样——只是比她更有理由——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯开始觉得，仿佛有一张网正朝他收紧。

终于，他拐进那条通向安克福德府的长车道；望见伊丽莎白时代风格的长长正面上灯火点点、灿然生辉，他的心猛地一跳。

他按响正门的门铃，随即强使自己耐心等候老博尔顿（Bolton）——此人当年是他父亲的马夫长，如今算是府里的总管，什么零活都干。

===Original===

But when, all at once, the door opened, it was his mother, tall, upright, grey-haired, who stood there, her face full of eager welcome.

“I knew it was you, my dearest! I don’t believe in presentiments, but I have been thinking of you all today, even more than usual.”

His face gave no answering smile. He looked very grave, and yet how young he seemed to her, standing there; how strong, how finely drawn and carved, was his now serious face!

“I wish you’d wired, Roger. Enid was coming in to late supper; but I’ll put her off﻿—”

“You needn’t do that, mother. It’s true I’ve come down to tell you of something rather unpleasant that’s just happened to me. But the telling of it won’t take long. Please don’t put off Enid. In fact I shall be glad to see her, and I may have to go back to town by the last train.”

===Chinese===

可是门倏地一下开了，站在那儿的是他母亲——身材高挑，腰背笔直，一头灰发，满脸热切的欢迎。

「我就知道是你，我最亲爱的！我本不信什么预感，可今天一整天我都惦着你，比平常惦得还厉害。」

他的脸上没有报以笑容。他神情非常凝重；然而在她眼里，站在那里的他显得多么年轻啊！那张此刻一本正经的脸，又是多么坚毅，线条多么清俊有力！

「你要是拍个电报来就好了，罗杰。伊妮德要来吃夜宵；不过我可以把她推掉——」

「用不着，母亲。我确实是为一件刚落到我头上的不愉快的事下来的。不过讲起来用不了多久。请别推掉伊妮德。说实话，见到她我还高兴呢，说不定我还得赶末班车回城里去。」

===Original===

He followed her across the wide hall which formed the centre of the old house, and so into a lobby which led to the charming sitting-room which had always been associated in his mind with his mother. They both sat down there. But he waited a moment before he began his story and then in the telling of it he chose his words with painful care.

“A very odd thing has happened, mother, and I felt I should like to tell you about it at once.”

“What is it that has happened, Roger?”

As he said nothing, she went on quietly, in a matter-of-fact tone: “Whatever it is, I know quite well that you have not been to blame in any way.”

“Well, no, I don’t think I have been to blame. And yet, well, mother, I’ve not been﻿—” and then he stopped dead.

===Chinese===

他跟着她穿过构成老宅中心的宽阔大厅，走进一方门厅，由此通往那间雅致的起居室——在他心里，这间屋子从来就同母亲连在一起。两人在那里坐定。他却静待了片刻方才开口，而讲述的当口，他字斟句酌，用心良苦。

「母亲，出了一桩很离奇的事，我觉得该马上告诉您。」

「出了什么事，罗杰？」

他一言不发，她便平静地接着说下去，语气就事论事：「不管是什么事，我都很清楚，你无论如何是没有过错的。」

「是啊，我也觉得自己没有过错。可是，唉，母亲，我一直不曾——」他猛地打住了。

===Original===

For the first time in his life he felt afraid. The extraordinary story he had come to tell suddenly took on gigantic proportions. Until today, though he had felt discomfort, and something akin to shame, sometimes, when with Jervis Lexton, Roger Gretorex and Fear had never met.

“You remember,” he said at last, “my friend Ivy Lexton? She came down here for a weekend last winter.”

“I remember Mrs. Lexton very well,” answered Mrs. Gretorex in a tone of studious detachment.

As her son had uttered the name of the woman he called his friend, a feeling of fear coupled with a sensation of painful jealousy filled the mother’s heart. Remember the beautiful woman she had instantly known, without his telling her so, that Roger loved? There had scarcely been a day in the last few months when she had not remembered, with a sensation of discomfort, lovely Ivy Lexton.

“Jervis Lexton, Ivy’s husband, fell ill about three weeks ago﻿—”

===Chinese===

他有生以来第一次感到了害怕。他下来要讲的那桩离奇故事，忽然间膨胀得庞大无比。直到今天为止，虽然同杰维斯·莱克斯顿相处时，他有时也觉过不安、觉过近乎羞惭的东西，可罗杰·格雷托雷克斯还从不曾与「恐惧」打过照面。

「您还记得吧，」他终于开口，「我的朋友艾薇·莱克斯顿？去年冬天她来这儿度过了一个周末。」

「莱克斯顿太太我记得很清楚。」格雷托雷克斯太太答道，语气刻意地淡漠。

儿子一说出那个他称作朋友的女人的名字，做母亲的心里便涌起一阵恐惧，夹着一种痛楚的嫉妒。记得那个美丽的女人？当年他连一个字都不曾说破，她就一眼看穿罗杰爱的是她！这几个月来，几乎没有一天她不想起妩媚动人的艾薇·莱克斯顿，而且每次想起，都伴着一阵说不出的不适。

「艾薇的丈夫杰维斯·莱克斯顿，约莫三个礼拜前病倒了——」

===Original===

And then again Gretorex felt as if he could not go on.

“What has happened is put as clearly, here, as anything I can tell you!” he exclaimed at last, and he handed her the evening paper containing Ivy’s photograph.

She took the paper from his hand, and she was in such haste to see what it was that her son did not dare to tell her himself, that she did not wait to put on her spectacles.

Holding the sheet right under her reading lamp, she read the ominous paragraphs headed “A Kensington Poisoning Mystery” right through.

“Well,” she said at last, “and in what way, Roger, does this concern you? Were you acting as Mr. Lexton’s medical attendant?”

===Chinese===

说到这里，格雷托雷克斯又觉得难以为继。

「事情的经过，这上头写得一清二楚，比我能告诉您的还清楚！」他终于喊出声来，把那张印着艾薇照片的晚报递了过去。

她从儿子手里接过报纸，急着想看到儿子不敢亲口告诉她的究竟是什么，连眼镜都顾不上戴。

她把报纸直凑在阅读灯下，把那篇题为「肯辛顿毒杀疑云」的不祥报道，从头到尾读了一遍。

「唔，」她终于开口，「罗杰，这件事同你有什么相干？你是给莱克斯顿先生当主治医生来着？」

===Original===

He answered at once, “I’m glad to say I was not. In fact I only saw the poor chap twice during the whole course of his illness. He was being looked after by a very good doctor, a man called Berwick.”

She said again, “Then in what way does this horrible story concern you, my dear?”

There followed a long pause, and all at once a certain suspicion rushed into Mrs. Gretorex’s mind.

“Is it possible,” she said at last, in a very low voice, “that your friend Mrs. Lexton is suspected of having poisoned her husband?”

Roger Gretorex leapt to his feet.

===Chinese===

他马上回答：「幸好不是。其实他生病的整个期间，我统共只见过那可怜人两回。照看他的是一位很好的医生，姓伯威克。」

她又问：「那么，我亲爱的，这个可怕的故事究竟同你有什么相干呢？」

接着是长久的沉默，忽然间，某种猜疑猛地涌上格雷托雷克斯太太的心头。

「会不会，」她终于开口，声音压得极低，「是你的朋友莱克斯顿太太，被人怀疑毒死了她的丈夫？」

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯霍地跳了起来。

===Original===

“Good God﻿—no, mother! Whatever made you think of such a thing?”

“I don’t know. Forgive me, Roger.”

For the first time in her life she felt that her son was looking at her with something like﻿—oh, no, not hatred, but anger, furious anger, in his blazing eyes.

He repeated the cruel question: “Whatever made such a monstrous idea come into your mind?”

She faltered, “It was foolish of me.”

“More than foolish﻿—and very unlike you, mother,” he said harshly.

===Chinese===

「天哪——不，母亲！您怎么会想到这种事上头去？」

「我也不知道。原谅我，罗杰。」

她有生以来第一次觉得，儿子望着她的眼神里含着某种近乎——哦不，不是憎恨，而是愤怒，是熊熊的怒火，在他灼灼的双眼里燃烧。

他又把那句残酷的问话重复了一遍：「您脑子里怎么会生出这样荒唐的念头？」

她嗫嚅道：「是我糊涂。」

「岂止是糊涂——母亲，这可太不像您了。」他声色俱厉地说。

===Original===

Then he moved his chair closer to hers, and stretching out his hand, he took hers.

“Ivy was the best of wives to Jervis Lexton,” he said in a low voice. “Lexton ran through a large fortune, and then, instead of trying to get a job, simply idled about, and lived on his friends. He was a complete wastrel.”

“Then isn’t what the paper says true?” she asked in bewilderment. “I mean about his having joined the firm of Miles Rushworth? I thought the Rushworths were shipping millionaires?”

“So they are. And it’s quite true that Lexton had just got a job in the Rushworths’ London office. He was well connected, and had a lot of good-natured friends who were always trying to get him something to do. However﻿—” and then he quoted the familiar Latin tag concerning ill words of the dead.

She gazed across at him. His dark face, now convulsed with feeling, was partly illumined by the lamp which stood on a low table between them.

===Chinese===

随后他把椅子挪到母亲跟前，伸出手去，握住了她的手。

「艾薇对杰维斯·莱克斯顿，是再好不过的妻子，」他低声说，「莱克斯顿把一大份家产挥霍精光，之后非但不去谋个差事，反倒四处游荡，靠朋友养活。他是个十足的败家子。」

「那么报上说的不是真的了？」她茫然地问道，「我是说他进迈尔斯·拉什沃思公司做事那段。我还以为拉什沃思家是航运界的百万富翁呢？」

「他们是。莱克斯顿刚在拉什沃思轮船公司的伦敦办事处谋到差事，这也是真的。他人头熟，一帮好心的朋友总想给他找点事做。不过——」说到这里，他引了那句家喻户晓的拉丁古谚，说的正是对死者不可出恶言。

她凝望着他。他那张黝黑的脸因心绪激荡而微微抽搐，被立在两人之间一张矮桌上的灯照亮了一半。

===Original===

“Is it conceivable, my son﻿—”

Then she, like him, stopped short, afraid to utter the words she was going to say.

“Yes, mother?”

His voice had suddenly become listless. He had dropped her hand, and was lying back in his chair. He was feeling spent, worn out.

“Have you any reason to suppose, my boy, that you are in danger of being accused of having poisoned Jervis Lexton?”

===Chinese===

「这可能吗，我的孩子——」

接着她同他一样戛然收声，不敢把要说的话说出口。

「怎么，母亲？」

他的声音突然变得恹恹的。他松开了她的手，仰靠在椅背上。他只觉得心力交瘁。

「我的孩子，你有没有理由认为，你自己有被控毒杀杰维斯·莱克斯顿的危险？」

===Original===

He straightened himself, got up, and then gazed down into her pale but still calm face, and she saw that he looked, if surprised, yet unutterably relieved.

“Yes, mother! That is what I came down to tell you. But what made you hit on the truth?”

Should she tell him the reason why that frightful thought had come into her mind? After a moment of indecision, she decided that she ought to do so.

“Can’t you guess why that fearful suspicion came into my mind, Roger?”

His eyes fell before her sad, steady, questioning gaze.

She went on slowly, “I said a word to you the evening of the day you brought Mrs. Lexton down here. I suppose you didn’t take my advice?”

===Chinese===

他挺直身子，站了起来，俯视着她那张苍白却依旧镇定的脸；她看得出，他虽说露出惊讶之色，却有一种难以言喻的如释重负。

「是的，母亲！我下来就是要告诉您这个。可您怎么一下就猜中了真相？」

要不要告诉他，那个可怕的念头为什么会钻进她心里？迟疑片刻之后，她认定还是应该说出来。

「你猜不出那个可怕的疑心是怎么钻进我心里来的吗，罗杰？」

在她哀伤、沉静、探询的目光注视下，他垂下了眼睛。

她缓缓地说下去：「你带莱克斯顿太太来这儿的那天晚上，我劝过你一句话。看来，你没有听我的劝？」

===Original===

“There are certain things about which a man must judge for himself, mother. And with regard to my friendship for Mrs. Lexton I judged for myself.”

He sat down again and covered his eyes with his right hand. The words he had just uttered had brought Ivy vividly before him.

“Now tell me everything, Roger.”

“There’s very little to tell,” said Gretorex, raising his head. “I feel sure, quite sure, mother, that there’s some perfectly natural explanation of what now seems so mysterious.”

“If, as I hope, you have come down to consult with me as to what is the best thing to do, then I trust that you will tell me the whole truth. After all, I am your mother, my darling.”

===Chinese===

「有些事情，母亲，男人必须自己拿主意。至于我同莱克斯顿太太的交情，我就是自己拿的主意。」

他重新坐下，右手掩住双眼。方才说出口的话，把艾薇活生生地带到了他眼前。

「现在把一切都告诉我吧，罗杰。」

「没什么可说的，」格雷托雷克斯抬起头说，「母亲，我深信，眼下显得如此神秘的事，自会有一个再自然不过的解释。」

「倘若如我所愿，你下来是要同我商量该怎么办才好，那么我相信，你会对我说出全部真相。归根到底，我是你的母亲啊，我的宝贝。」

===Original===

Her voice rose in entreaty.

“Whom should you trust, if you do not trust me?”

He felt very much moved, and, to her surprise, he came and knelt down by her.

She put her arms round him. “Now tell me everything,” she whispered.

And he did tell her almost everything. But he did not tell her, and he never told anyone, of his telephone talk with Ivy Lexton.

===Chinese===

她的声音带着恳求，高了起来。

「你连我都不信，还能信谁呢？」

他大为动容，而令她惊讶的是，他竟走过来跪在了她身旁。

她伸出双臂搂住他。「现在都告诉我吧。」她悄声说。

于是他果真把几乎一切都告诉了她。只是他没有告诉她——他也从未告诉过任何人——他同艾薇·莱克斯顿通电话那件事。

===Original===

His mother was quick to see the flaw in his apparently frank account of all that had happened that morning.

“How did you first hear of Mr. Lexton’s death?” she asked. “You were there, I note from the date given in this paper, on the afternoon of the day he died. You must in fact have gone straight to the station, from your unfortunate call on the poor man.”

“I did, mother.”

“Then how, and when, did you hear of his death, Roger?”

He got up and went across to the chair where he had been sitting.

===Chinese===

母亲很快就看出了，他对那天早晨发生的一切所作的那份看似坦率的叙述里，有一处破绽。

「你最初是怎么听说莱克斯顿先生的死讯的？」她问，「我从报上登的日期看出，他死的那天下午你就在伦敦。想必你是探望完那可怜人，就直接去了车站。」

「是的，母亲。」

「那么，罗杰，你是几点、又是怎么听说的呢？」

他起身走回方才坐的那把椅子。

===Original===

“I found a note from Ivy when I arrived at Ferry Place this morning. She knew I was away, and she sent me the note to await me on my return. It was written, evidently, some days ago.”

“I hope you have kept her note.”

He shook his head. “No, mother, I didn’t keep it. There was really nothing in it﻿—simply the bare statement that Jervis Lexton was dead. She had had an awful shock, and she said she didn’t want to see anyone.”

“Then you’ve not seen Mrs. Lexton since her husband’s death?”

“No, I have not seen her.”

===Chinese===

「今天早上我回到费里街时，发现了艾薇留的一张字条。她知道我不在城里，便把字条先送去，等我回来时看。看得出来，是几天前写的。」

「但愿那张字条你留着。」

他摇了摇头。「没有，母亲，我没有留。那上面实在什么都没有——只有一句干巴巴的话，说杰维斯·莱克斯顿死了。她受了可怕的打击，说她谁也不想见。」

「那么她丈夫死后，你没再见过莱克斯顿太太？」

「没有，我没有见过她。」

===Original===

There trembled on the poor mother’s lips a further question﻿—“Or had any further communication with her?” But she feared he might be tempted to tell her a lie, so she refrained from asking that, to her, important question.

Instead she said, “Though you have not put it into words, you feel sure that the man from Scotland Yard suspects you poisoned Jervis Lexton so that his wife would be free to marry you?”

“That is certainly what the inspector had in his mind, when he questioned me as to my acquaintance with her.”

“Forgive me for asking you the question, Roger. I suppose you would marry Mrs. Lexton, if you had the chance?”

He said at once, “I’d give my soul to marry her, mother. I love her﻿—love her more, I think, than man ever loved woman.”

===Chinese===

一个更深的问题，在这位可怜母亲的唇边颤动——「也没有别的往来？」可她怕儿子会受引诱，对她说出谎话，终于忍住没有问出这个在她看来要紧的问题。

她换了个说法：「虽然你没有明说，可你认定，苏格兰场来的人怀疑你毒杀了杰维斯·莱克斯顿，好让他的妻子得以嫁给你？」

「他盘问我同她的交往时，心里想的肯定就是这个。」

「原谅我问你这个问题，罗杰。要是有机会，你是会娶莱克斯顿太太的吧？」

他应声答道：「娶她？我情愿拿灵魂去换，母亲。我爱她——我想，男人爱女人，从来没有爱到像我这样的。」

===Original===

She gave him a searching look. “Does she love you?”

He hesitated, painfully. “I’ve seen very little of her lately. I know she felt we were not doing right in seeing as much as we were seeing of each other.”

And then he sighed, a long, long sigh.

“Try to love her, mother. She is free now, and she is all my life. Please, please try to love her for my sake.”

“I will, my darling boy.”

Mrs. Gretorex was ashamed of the hatred﻿—she acknowledged to herself that it had been hatred﻿—of Ivy Lexton which had filled her heart. She now told herself that this woman whom her boy adored must have some good in her.

===Chinese===

她用锐利的目光打量他。「她爱你吗？」

他痛苦地迟疑着。「近来我很少见到她。我知道，她觉得我们那样频频相见是不对的。」

随后他叹了口气，长长的一叹。

「试着去爱她吧，母亲。她如今自由了，她是我生命的全部。求您了，求您看在我的份上，试着去爱她。」

「我会的，我亲爱的孩子。」

格雷托雷克斯太太为充塞自己心头的那种对艾薇·莱克斯顿的憎恨——她向自己承认，那一直是憎恨——感到羞愧。如今她告诉自己，儿子奉为至宝的这个女人，身上总归有她的好处。

===Original===

There came the sound of the front door opening.

“Here, I think, is Enid Dent,” exclaimed Mrs. Gretorex.

Roger jumped up from his chair, and went forward to meet the girl who, as he knew deep in his heart, loved him.

Was it because he had known that his mother eagerly desired him to marry Enid, or was it simply because he knew her too well? Be that as it may, it had only taken three meetings with Ivy Lexton, the wife of another man, to blot Enid out from his heart.

Today, the most terrible day of his life, the sight of her honest, thoughtful face brought comfort. For one thing, it was such an infinite relief to know that in Enid Dent his mother would have an entirely trustworthy, devoted support and stay, during days which he feared must be anxious and painful days, terrible days to remember, though he had no doubt at all as to the ultimate result of any inquiry into the facts surrounding Lexton’s death.

As for Enid Dent, she had loved Roger Gretorex with a silent, unswerving devotion since she had first known what love meant. Though he himself was scarcely aware of it, his whole manner had changed during the last few months, and while this caused her sharp anguish, which she had successfully hidden from those about her, it had never occurred to her, strangely enough, that that alteration had come about because of another woman.

===Chinese===

传来前门开门的声音。

「这想必是伊妮德·登特来了。」格雷托雷克斯太太失声道。

罗杰从椅子上跳起来，迎上前去见那位姑娘——他在心底深处知道，她是爱着他的。

是因为他早知道母亲巴望他娶伊妮德呢，还是仅仅因为他太了解伊妮德了？无论如何，同另一个男人的妻子艾薇·莱克斯顿只见过三面，伊妮德便从他心里被抹去了。

今天，他一生中最可怕的一天，见到她那张诚实而多思的脸，反倒给他带来了慰藉。其一，往后那些他担心必然充满焦灼与痛苦的日子——那些日后回想起来可怕的日子——一想到母亲身边有伊妮德·登特这么一个完全可靠、忠心耿耿的依靠和支柱，便是莫大的宽慰；尽管对围绕莱克斯顿之死的种种事实的任何调查，其最终结果如何，他心里丝毫不曾怀疑过。

至于伊妮德·登特，打从她初解爱情为何物起，就一直以一种沉默而坚定不移的深情，爱着罗杰·格雷托雷克斯。他自己虽几乎不曾察觉，最近几个月来他整个人的举止都变了；这给了她锥心的痛楚——她成功地瞒过了身边所有的人——可是说来奇怪，她从未想到，这变化是因另一个女人而起。

===Original===

Now, gazing from the mother to the son, she understood at once that they were both in deep trouble.

“I’m afraid I’ve come too soon,” she said.

“I’m glad you came early, my dear. We’re going to have supper in a few minutes, for Roger has to go back to town tonight.”

Old Bolton hobbled into the room.

“Rosie Holt says you promised to see her some time today, ma’am. She’s in the kitchen. Shall I show her into the drawing-room?”

Mrs. Gretorex made a great effort over herself.

===Chinese===

此刻，她望望母亲又望望儿子，立刻明白母子俩都深陷烦恼。

「恐怕我来得太早了。」她说。

「你来得早我才高兴呢，亲爱的。几分钟以后我们就吃夜宵，罗杰今晚还得回城里去。」

老博尔顿一瘸一拐地走进屋来。

「罗西·霍尔特（Rosie Holt）说，您答应了今天什么时候见见她，太太。她这会儿在厨房里。要不要我把她让到客厅去？」

格雷托雷克斯太太费了好大的劲，才按住自己的情绪。

===Original===

“Yes, please do, Bolton,” she said quietly, “and I’ll come and see her.”

Roger turned to Enid:

“I wonder if you’d take a short turn before supper? I don’t get nearly enough exercise in London.”

“Of course I will.”

They went into the hall, and she hurried on her hat and coat. It was fully a year since she had last taken even a short walk with Roger Gretorex.

===Chinese===

「好的，请带她去吧，博尔顿，」她平静地说，「我这就去见她。」

罗杰转向伊妮德：

「饭前你可肯同我稍稍走一走？我在伦敦的运动量实在少得可怜。」

「当然肯。」

两人走进大厅，她匆匆戴上帽子、披上外套。她同罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，连短短一次散步，也整整一年不曾有了。

===Original===

Once they were in the open air, in the kindly darkness, he drew her arm through his.

“Enid, I want to tell you something.”

He spoke almost in a whisper﻿—as if he were afraid he might be overheard.

“Yes, Roger?” and she slightly pressed his arm.

“I’m in trouble,” he said sombrely; “in great trouble, my dear. What makes it worse is the knowledge of how unhappy it is going to make my mother.”

And then at once, for though she was young she was no fool﻿—your country-bred girl often knows a great deal more of real life than your town-bred girl﻿—Enid Dent said to herself that Roger’s trouble was connected with a woman.

===Chinese===

一到户外，到了那善解人意的黑暗里，他便把她的手臂挽进自己臂弯。

「伊妮德，我有件事要告诉你。」

他说话几乎是耳语——仿佛怕被人偷听了去似的。

「嗯，罗杰？」她轻轻握了握他的手臂。

「我遇到麻烦了，」他郁郁地说，「大麻烦，亲爱的。更糟的是，我知道这会让我母亲多么难过。」

伊妮德·登特当即对自己说——她虽年轻，却不是傻子；乡下长大的姑娘，比起城里长大的姑娘，往往更懂得真正的生活——罗杰的麻烦，同一个女人有关。

===Original===

“Tell me all about it,” she said quietly, and she stiffened herself to bear a blow.

“I will tell you all about it. But I’m afraid you will be very much shocked.”

To that she made no answer. No doubt some London girl was bringing a breach of promise action against Roger. That was the sort of trouble Enid Dent visualised.

“Things are never so bad when one talks them over,” she said, and tried to smile in the dark night. “Nothing could make me feel any different to you, Roger. Why, you’re my oldest friend!”

They were walking away from the house, down a broad path where they had often played when he was a boy of twelve and she a little girl of five.

“No talking can make any difference to my trouble,” and there came a harsh note in his voice.

===Chinese===

「都讲给我听吧。」她平静地说，同时绷紧了身子，准备承受打击。

「我会都讲给你听的。只怕你会大吃一惊。」

对这话她没有作声。多半是哪个伦敦姑娘要对罗杰提起毁弃婚约（breach of promise）的诉讼了吧。伊妮德·登特心里设想的，就是那一类的麻烦。

「事情说出来，总没有想的那么糟。」她说，还想在黑夜里笑一笑。「无论什么都改变不了我对你的看法，罗杰。你可是我最老的朋友了！」

两人正朝背离宅子的方向走去，沿着一条宽阔的小径——他十二岁、她五岁那年，他们常在这条路上一起玩耍。

「说什么也化解不了我的麻烦。」他的嗓音里透出一股粗粝。

===Original===

They walked along in silence, and then he gently shook himself free of her arm.

“I’ve reason to believe that in the next few days﻿—”

He stopped short. The ignominy, the horror of what might be going to happen to him, overwhelmed him.

“Yes, Roger? What is it that you think is going to happen? Tell me.”

He would have been surprised, indeed, if she had suddenly uttered aloud the words that she was saying in her heart, “Don’t you see the agony I am in? It’s cruel, cruel to keep me in suspense!”

“I think it possible, perhaps I ought to say likely, that I may be arrested on a charge of murder, within the next few days.” He uttered the dread words quietly enough. “And that though I assure you, Enid, that I am absolutely innocent﻿—”

===Chinese===

两人默默走了一程，随后他轻轻挣脱了她的手臂。

「我有理由相信，往后几天里——」

他戛然收声。那可能落到他头上的羞辱与恐怖，把他压倒了。

「嗯，罗杰？你觉得要出什么事？告诉我。」

倘若她突然出声，说出她心里正说着的话，他准会大吃一惊：「你看不出我是在怎样的煎熬里吗？这样吊着我，太残忍了，太残忍了！」

「我想有可能——或许我该说，多半是——往后几天之内，我会以谋杀的罪名被捕。」他说出这句可怕的话来，语气倒是够平静的。「而我向你保证，伊妮德，我是绝对清白的——」

===Original===

She cut across his words, “You needn’t have troubled to tell me that, Roger.”

“Though I’m absolutely innocent,” he repeated, “yet I’m beginning to realise that appearances are very much against me. I’ve felt all this afternoon as if I were living through a frightful nightmare, and I’m always expecting to wake up and find it was only a dream, after all.”

Enid knew that this man she loved had a violent temper. She supposed that he had had the kind of quarrel with another man in which one of the two strikes out.

“I’ve been wondering, during the last few minutes, whether you would be able to come up to London with my mother? It would be such a comfort to know you were with her, if this thing really happens.”

“Of course I will!” she exclaimed.

He went on: “You’ve always been like a daughter to her, haven’t you? And I know that, next to me, she loves you best in the world.”

===Chinese===

她打断了他的话：「罗杰，这你还用得着告诉我？」

「虽然我绝对清白，」他重复道，「可我开始明白，种种表象对我大大不利。今天一下午，我都觉得自己像在经历一场骇人的噩梦，总盼着醒过来，发现这一切到头来不过是一场梦。」

伊妮德知道，她所爱的这个人脾气暴烈。她猜想，他多半是同哪个男人起了那类口角，其中一方动了手。

「刚才这一路，我一直在想，你能不能陪我母亲一道上伦敦来？倘若那件事真的发生了，知道你陪着她，我就宽心多了。」

「我当然愿意！」她叫道。

他接着说：「你一向就像她的亲生女儿，不是吗？我知道，这个世界上，除了我，她最爱的就是你。」

===Original===

“I think she does,” she whispered in a strangled voice, for she was now near to tears. “But a long way after you, Roger.”

“Well, yes,” he answered, in a matter-of-fact tone; “no doubt a long way after me. But still she loves you dearly, and she trusts you utterly, as I do.”

“I’ll stay with her all through the trouble﻿—if the trouble comes. I promise you that, Roger.”

“It’s not that I have any doubt as to the outcome. The man whose death is being, I feel sure, put down to my account, almost certainly committed suicide. There’s no other solution possible.”

“Who was the man?” she asked diffidently.

“An acquaintance rather than a friend of mine, called Jervis Lexton. He fell ill﻿—that I have to admit is a mysterious point about the whole business﻿—about a fortnight ago. He died, rather suddenly, last week, on the 16th. A postmortem revealed the cause of death to have been a virulent poison﻿—arsenic. I saw him alone a few hours before he died, and I have a jar of arsenic in my surgery. There you have the story in a nutshell.”

===Chinese===

「我想是的。」她哽咽着低声说，因为这时她已几乎要落泪。「不过远远排在你后头，罗杰。」

「哦，那自然，」他实事求是地答道，「无疑远远排在我后头。可她照样疼你，而且她完全信得过你，同我一样。」

「这桩麻烦里——倘若麻烦当真来了——我会一直陪着她。我向你保证，罗杰。」

「倒不是我对结局有什么怀疑。那个人的死，我敢说，眼下正要算到我的账上，可他几乎可以肯定是自尽的。此外别无可能的解释。」

「那人是谁？」她怯怯地问。

「与其说是朋友，不如说是我的一个熟人，叫杰维斯·莱克斯顿。他病倒了——这一点我不得不承认，是整件事里神秘的一环——约莫两个星期以前。他上礼拜去世了，死得相当突然，就在16日那天。验尸查明，死因是一种烈性毒药——砒霜。死前几个钟头，我单独见过他，而我的诊疗室里就放着一罐砒霜。事情的原委，就这么几句。」

===Original===

He spoke in an awkward, constrained tone.

“Are you really going back tonight?” she asked. “Can’t you stay till tomorrow morning? It would be such a comfort to Mrs. Gretorex.”

“I’m afraid I must go back. You see, I’ve a lot of patients, and it isn’t fair to put all that work on another doctor.”

“I see.”

“I wonder if you and my mother can come to town tomorrow? She’d be wretched, staying on here in suspense, waiting for news of what, after all, may never happen.”

===Chinese===

他说话的口气局促而拘谨。

「你当真今夜就要回去？」她问，「不能待到明天早上吗？那对格雷托雷克斯太太该是多大的安慰啊。」

「恐怕我必须回去。你是知道的，我有一大群病人，把那些活儿全压到另一位医生身上，说不过去。」

「我明白了。」

「你和我母亲明天能不能进城来？她要是留在这儿悬着心，等那件——说到底——也许永远不会发生的事的消息，会愁坏的。」

===Original===

They were turning now towards the house, and as they emerged from under the trees they both noticed that the front door was open. Through it a shaft of bright light fell on the stone-paved courtyard, and Gretorex suddenly became aware that, in the shadow, a motor with hooded lights was drawn up.

“Who can that be at this time of the evening?” he said, surprised.

They walked swiftly across the wide lawn, and so on to the stone pavement. Then, as they passed through the open door, they heard Mrs. Gretorex’s voice and the unfamiliar voice of a man in the great hall.

“I think I hear my son coming in. In any case, I assure you he won’t be long.”

Mrs. Gretorex uttered the words in a matter-of-fact yet anxious tone, as if she feared the person she spoke to might not believe her.

===Chinese===

这时两人正转身朝宅子走去；刚从树影底下钻出来，他们便都注意到前门敞着。一道明亮的光从门里泻到石板铺就的院子上，格雷托雷克斯蓦地觉察到，暗处停着一辆遮了车灯的汽车。

「这个时辰还会是谁呢？」他诧异地说。

两人快步穿过宽阔的草坪，踏上石板路。正当他们穿过敞开的大门时，听见大客厅里传来格雷托雷克斯太太的声音，还有一个陌生男人的声音。

「我想是我儿子回来了。不管怎样，我向您担保，他去不了多久。」

格雷托雷克斯太太说这话时，语气既像就事论事，又透着焦灼，仿佛生怕同她说话的那人不肯相信似的。

===Original===

Roger followed Enid through the lobby which separated the front door from the hall, and then he saw his mother standing with a tall, slight man whom Roger knew to be the Inspector of Police at Lynchester, the county town hard by. They had met two months back in connection with a local poaching affray.

“May I speak with you for a few moments in private, Dr. Gretorex?”

A look of great relief had come over the inspector’s face; he was aware in what high regard Mrs. Gretorex was held throughout the neighbourhood. He had also noticed the young lady who had just come in, and knew her for the only daughter of a local magistrate. So he was anxious to get through the unpleasant business which had brought him tonight to Anchorford Hall, as quietly and quickly as possible.

“I’m quite at your service. We’ll go into the smoking-room, but﻿—”

Gretorex turned right round and began rapidly walking towards the front door.

===Chinese===

罗杰跟着伊妮德穿过隔在前门与大厅之间的门厅，随即看见母亲同一个瘦高个的男人站在一处——罗杰知道，那是邻近郡治林彻斯特（Lynchester）的警察探长。两个月前，为着本地一桩盗猎械斗（poaching affray），两人打过交道。

「可否同您私下谈几分钟，格雷托雷克斯医生？」

探长脸上现出如释重负的神情；他知道格雷托雷克斯太太在这一带如何受人敬重。他也留意到刚进来的那位年轻小姐，认得她是本地一位治安官的独生女。于是他急于把他今夜到安克福德府来的这桩不愉快的差事，办得越安静、越利落越好。

「我听凭您吩咐。我们去吸烟室谈吧，不过——」

格雷托雷克斯倏地转过身，快步朝前门走去。

===Original===

As a matter of fact, the door had been left open, and he wished to close it.

But the inspector believed his lawful prey intended to escape into the darkness, and a hundred suspicious, angry thoughts flashed through his mind.

What a thing it would be to have to search the downs and woods all this coming night! ’Twould be like looking for a needle in a stack of hay.

He strode past Mrs. Gretorex, and seized Roger with no gentle hand by the collar.

“I’m surprised, sir, at your trying to get away. I didn’t expect such a thing from you!”

Gretorex wrenched himself free.

===Chinese===

事实上，那扇门本就敞着，他不过是想去把它关上。

可探长认定，他这只合法的猎物想要遁入黑暗，一时间，百般疑惧与愤懑的念头在他脑子里电闪而过。

今夜接下来还得搜遍丘原和树林，这差事可怎么当！那无异于大海捞针。

他大步抢过格雷托雷克斯太太跟前，一把毫不客气地揪住罗杰的领口。

「您竟想脱身，这倒叫我吃惊，先生！我可没有料到您会来这一手！」

格雷托雷克斯猛地挣脱开。

===Original===

“I don’t know what you mean!” he exclaimed angrily.

“Oh, yes, you do. You were making for that door.”

“I was making for the door to shut it.”

He was shaking with anger, and the two glared at each other for a moment in silence.

Then the inspector took a step forward, and laid his hand on the young man’s arm.

“I arrest you,” he said, in a voice that was not quite steady, “on the charge of having murdered Jervis Lexton on the 16th of this month.”

===Chinese===

「我不明白您这是什么意思！」他怒气冲冲地喊道。

「哦，您明白。您方才直奔那扇门去。」

「我奔那扇门去，是去关门的。」

他气得发抖，两人默然对峙了片刻，怒目相向。

随后探长跨前一步，把手按在年轻人的臂上。

「你被捕了，」他说，声音也不大稳，「罪名是本月16日谋杀杰维斯·莱克斯顿。」

===Original===

Roger Gretorex stood still. Then he asked:

“May I speak to my mother in private for a moment?”

“No,” said the inspector quickly. “I cannot allow you to do that, Dr. Gretorex. I’m sorry, but from now on you are my prisoner.”

“May I make a statement to you now? I suppose there is no objection to my telling you that I’m absolutely innocent?”

The older man hesitated.

“I should advise you,” he said, not unkindly, “to make no statement. You are, of course, aware that anything you say may be used against you in evidence. I need hardly tell you that every facility will be given you to procure legal advice.”

===Chinese===

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯伫立不动。随后他问：

「可否容我同母亲单独说几句话？」

「不行，」探长疾声道，「这个我不能准许，格雷托雷克斯医生。很抱歉，从现在起，你是我的囚犯了。」

「我现在可否向你作一项声明？我猜，我声明自己绝对清白，你该没有异议吧？」

年长的人迟疑了。

「我劝你，」他的口气并不苛刻，「什么声明也别作。你当然清楚，你说出口的任何话，都可能被用作不利于你的证据。用不着我多说：一切便利都会给你，让你得以延请法律顾问。」

===Original===

“And what is going to happen to me now?”

“You will go with me to Lynchester, and you will be kept there in a police cell till you are conveyed to London tomorrow. Once there, as you probably are aware, Dr. Gretorex, you will be taken to the police station of the district where the alleged murder was committed, and in due course you will be charged.”

Meanwhile the inspector was watching his prisoner closely. He was remembering that during the brief telephone conversation with Scotland Yard, which had led to his presence here, he had been reminded how near Anchorford was to the sea, and he had been warned that he might find his bird flown.

What a fool he would look if, after having actually arrested him, this man effected even a temporary escape!

“May I shake hands with my mother and﻿—and with my friends?”

“I will take it on myself to allow you to do that, Dr. Gretorex,” was the cold reply. “Then, I’m afraid, we must be getting on.”

===Chinese===

「那么我眼下会怎么样？」

「你随我去林彻斯特，先关在警察局的牢房里，明天再解往伦敦。到了那边——你多半也知道，格雷托雷克斯医生——你会被押送到案发地段的警察局，到时候按程序对你正式提出指控。」

这其间，探长一直紧盯着他的囚犯。他记得，先前同苏格兰场那次简短的电话通话——正是那通电话引得他今夜到此——对方提醒过他，安克福德离海多么近，还警告过他，他这只鸟儿兴许已经飞了。

倘若这人当真被捕之后，还能闹出一场哪怕暂时的脱逃，他这张脸可往哪儿搁！

「可否容我同母亲握握手——还有——还有同我的朋友们握握手？」

「这个我就自作主张准许了，格雷托雷克斯医生，」回答冷冷的。「那么，恕我直言，我们该动身了。」

===Original===

“Won’t you allow my son to have some supper before you take him away?” asked Mrs. Gretorex. For the first time her voice was not quite steady. “Won’t you both have supper here? It’s quite ready.”

“No, ma’am, I’m afraid I can’t do that. But I promise you your son shall have something to eat, as good as I can get him at this time of night, when we reach Lynchester.”

The inspector’s voice had become kindly, even respectful, to his prisoner’s mother. He felt very sorry for her.

But for Roger Gretorex he was not at all sorry. He had been given to understand, quite unofficially of course, that there was a married woman in the case, and that she provided a strong enough motive to hang a dozen times over the fine young fellow now standing by his side.

“What I would advise you to do, ma’am﻿—advising you as a private person, I mean﻿—would be to go up to London tomorrow morning, and get in touch with a good solicitor. Dr. Gretorex will be allowed to see his lawyer alone as much as he can reasonably require. At least that is the usual procedure.”

===Chinese===

「带他走之前，能不能让我儿子吃口夜宵？」格雷托雷克斯太太问道。她的声音第一次不大稳了。「你们二位要不要也在这儿用些？都现成着呢。」

「不了，夫人，恕难从命。不过我答应您，到了林彻斯特，我给您儿子弄点吃的——夜里这个时辰，能弄到什么就算什么好的。」

对囚徒的母亲，探长的口气变得和气了，甚至带着敬意。他实在为她难过。

可是对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，他一点也不同情。他得到过风声——当然，全然是非官方的——说案子里有个有夫之妇，而她提供的动机，足够把这个此刻站在他身边的出色年轻人，送上十几回绞架。

「我劝您做的，夫人——我这是以私人身份进言——是明天一早上伦敦，去寻一位好律师。格雷托雷克斯医生将被允许在合理需要的范围内，单独会见他的律师。至少惯例是这样。」

===Original===

Roger Gretorex held out his hand. Something seemed to warn him that it would be wiser for him to remain standing exactly where he was standing now. He felt that the inspector was watching him intently.

Mrs. Gretorex took a step forward. She shook hands quietly, unemotionally, with her son.

And then something very unexpected happened﻿—unexpected, that is, by every one of the four people there.

Enid Dent approached Roger a little timidly. Had he not, a few moments ago, called her his friend? When she was close to him, she looked up into his face, for he was far taller than she. And then, all at once, he bent forward and, putting his arms round her, he kissed her goodbye.

===Chinese===

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯伸出手去。冥冥中似有什么在警告他：还是站在原地一动不动为明智。他觉得探长正目不转睛地盯着他。

格雷托雷克斯太太上前一步。她同儿子握了握手，安静地、不动声色地握了握。

接着便发生了一件非常出人意料的事——在场的四个人，没有一个料到。

伊妮德·登特略带怯意地向罗杰走近。几分钟之前，他不是还称她为朋友吗？走到他跟前时，她仰起脸望着他，因为他比她高出许多。随后，他蓦地俯下身来，伸出双臂搂住她，同她吻别。

## XII / 十二

===Original===

As Ivy stepped down out of the telephone box, after her conversation with Roger Gretorex, she felt, though partially relieved, yet at the same time agitated and still terribly frightened. She was, indeed, so much affected that she did not even notice the admiring glance thrown at her by a man in the next box.

Her interview that morning with Inspector Orpington and his subordinate﻿—for he had brought with him a sergeant﻿—had made her feel sick with fear. True that, after she had answered with apparent frankness the first probing questions put to her, she had felt, as she almost always did feel with any man with whom life brought her into temporary contact, that the inspector was beginning to like her and to sympathise with her. But, even so, she had experienced this morning what she had never experienced before﻿—not only a sensation of abject fear, but also as if she were becoming entangled in a horrible, close-meshed net.

During a brief visit to Paris, in the old days when she and Jervis still had plenty of money to burn, she had gone with a gay party to the Grand Guignol. There she had seen acted a terrifying little play which showed the walls of a room closing in on a man. That was exactly how she had felt during the long examination and cross-examination she had endured this morning.

===Chinese===

同罗杰·格雷托雷克斯通完电话，艾薇从电话亭里跨出来，虽说松了几分心，却又心绪翻腾，仍怕得要命。她着实方寸大乱，连邻亭里一个男人投来的倾慕眼光都未曾察觉。

那天上午，奥平顿探长（Inspector Orpington）带着下属——他还领了一名警佐（sergeant）同来——对她进行的那场问话，叫她怕得心里发呕。诚然，在以表面的坦率应付过头几记试探的提问之后，她生出了一种感觉，几乎每一次命运把她同一个男人短暂地抛在一处时她都会生出的那种感觉：这位探长开始喜欢她、怜惜她了。然而即便如此，今天上午她仍旧尝到了生平未尝过的滋味——不单是一股卑怯的恐惧，更仿佛正被一张可怕的大网缠住，网眼细密，愈收愈紧。

早年间她和杰维斯还有的是大把可烧的钱，曾去巴黎小住，随一伙兴冲冲的游伴进过大木偶剧场（Grand Guignol）。在那里她看过一出骇人的小戏，演的是一间屋子的四壁步步向人合拢过来。今早那场她硬捱过来的漫长盘问与反复盘诘，给她的正是这么一种滋味。

===Original===

One of the things that had made her feel so dreadfully frightened was that the two men from Scotland Yard had not begun their investigations by seeing her, the widow of the dead man. They had first interviewed Nurse Bradfield, and then the cook.

While this had been going on, Ivy had waited in the drawing-room, sick with terror and suspense, wondering what the two women were saying about her. At long last, the strangers had come into the drawing-room, looking very grave indeed.

===Chinese===

使她怕得那样厉害的缘由之一是：苏格兰场来的这两个人，竟不是从她——死者的遗孀——身上着手查起，倒先见了布拉德菲尔德护士，随后又见了厨娘。

这一场进行的当口，艾薇在客厅里等候，吓得五内俱焚，悬着心猜那两个女人会说出她些什么来。好半天之后，那两个陌生人才走进客厅，神色着实凝重。

===Original===

And now, as she walked back to Duke of Kent Mansion, choosing instinctively a roundabout way, Ivy kept living over again that strange, even she had realised momentous, interview.

The inspector had gone straight to the point. When had she, Mrs. Jervis Lexton herself, last been in the company of her husband before his unexpected death? After an imperceptible pause, during which she was wondering fearfully if Nurse Bradfield had remembered all that had happened on that sinister last afternoon, she had answered the question truthfully. She said that she had been with Jervis after luncheon, while the nurse had gone out for a short time.

When Ivy had made this admission, there had come a look of alert questioning on the inspector’s face, for Nurse Bradfield had not mentioned that fact, which indeed she had forgotten. And then it was, on seeing the sudden change of countenance on the part of her inquisitor, that Mrs. Jervis Lexton had gently volunteered the statement that, after she herself had gone out, the sick man had had another visitor that afternoon, a friend of her own and her husband’s, a young man named Roger Gretorex, who was a doctor.

===Chinese===

此刻她走回肯特公爵大厦去，本能地拣了条绕远的路，一路上把那场古怪的问话——她自己也都明白事关重大——在心里一遍又一遍地重演。

探长开门见山。丈夫暴卒之前，她杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太本人最后一次同丈夫在一起，是在什么时候？她略作了一瞬难以觉察的停顿——那一瞬里她正惶惶地疑心，布拉德菲尔德护士可把那个不祥的最后下午桩桩件件都记全了——随后她照实答了这个问题。她说，午饭后护士出去过一小会儿，那阵子她陪着杰维斯。

艾薇这一承认，探长脸上顿时浮起一层警觉的探问之色，因为布拉德菲尔德护士并没有提到这一节——她也确实忘了。正是在瞧见盘问者神色骤变的那一刻，杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太才柔声主动补上一句：在她自己出门之后，那天下午病人还有过另一位来客，是她和她丈夫共同的朋友，一位名叫罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的年轻医生。

===Original===

She had allowed, and consciously allowed, herself to look embarrassed, as she made what sounded like an admission. As she had intended should be the case, the inspector had at once run after that hare. But she had not bargained for what had followed immediately﻿—insistent questioning as to her own and her husband’s relations with the man who had been, with the exception of the cook and the nurse, the last person to see Jervis Lexton alive.

How long had they known Dr. Gretorex? Did they see much of him? What had been her own relations with him? When, for instance, had she herself last seen him before the death of Jervis Lexton?

At last, when she was beginning to feel as if the meshes of the net were becoming smaller and smaller, he had “got out of her,” so Ivy put it to herself, that Roger Gretorex cared for her far more than a bachelor ought to care for the wife of a friend.

===Chinese===

她说这番听来宛如招供的话时，任由自己——存心任由自己——面露窘色。不出她的算计，探长当即追着这只兔子扑了上去。可紧随其后的局面却不曾打进她的算盘：对方穷追不舍，非要问个明白不可——她和她丈夫，同这个除厨娘与护士之外最后一个见到活着的杰维斯·莱克斯顿的人，究竟是什么关系。

他们认识格雷托雷克斯医生多久了？同他过从多吗？她本人同他是何种关系？比方说，杰维斯·莱克斯顿去世之前，她自己最后一次见他，又是在什么时候？

到后来，她已开始觉得网眼缩得愈来愈小，这时他总算从她嘴里「掏出了」——艾薇在心里这般措辞——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯对她的情意，远不止一个单身汉对朋友之妻所该有的那一份。

===Original===

Nevertheless, everything would now have been “quite all right” from Ivy’s point of view if it had stopped there. But to her dismay and surprise, Inspector Orpington suddenly began on quite another tack.

In spite of the fact, which he assured her he accepted as true, that she had rejected with indignation Dr. Gretorex’s advances, he suggested that it was odd that her own and her husband’s friendship with the young man had gone on. He “presumed” that Lexton had known nothing of Gretorex’s unwelcome attentions to Mrs. Lexton? Ivy had reluctantly admitted that that was so. And, as he pressed her, with one quick, probing question after another, she saw, with a clear, affrighted, inward vision, that what she had intended should be a molehill was growing into a mountain.

At last had come the most alarming query of all﻿—had she ever been to see Dr. Gretorex at 6 Ferry Place?

===Chinese===

尽管如此，倘若问到这里为止，在艾薇看来，一切本已「妥妥当当」了。孰料她惊愕交加：奥平顿探长突然换上了一套全然不同的路数。

她曾愤然回绝格雷托雷克斯医生的示爱，这一点他向她担保是信以为真的；可尽管如此，他却道：这情形之下，她们夫妇同这年轻人的交情居然还延续了下去，未免蹊跷。他「想来」，莱克斯顿对格雷托雷克斯向莱克斯顿太太大献的那些不受欢迎的殷勤，是毫不知情的吧？艾薇勉勉强强承认了正是如此。而随着他一个接一个迅疾刺人的问题逼将上来，她以一种清明而惊骇的内视之眼看得分明：原打算只作一座小土丘的，正眼看着隆成一座大山。

最后，来了那最叫人心惊胆战的一问——她可曾到费里街6号格雷托雷克斯医生的宅子里去见过他？

===Original===

All the questions put to her she had answered with apparent ease and frankness. And, as to the last, she explained that she had gone to Dr. Gretorex’s house with a great friend of hers, a lady who had then been a widow, a Mrs. Arundell, but who had married again, and was now in India. They had been accompanied by a young man who was a friend of Mrs. Arundell.

As to going out with Roger Gretorex, she had done so only occasionally, and never since he had confessed he loved her. No, never since her husband’s illness.

How deeply thankful was Ivy Lexton that she had really seen so little of Roger Gretorex of late!

===Chinese===

所有问到她的问题，她都答得表面从容而坦白。至于最后一问，她解释说，她是同一位至交女友一道去格雷托雷克斯医生家的——那位太太当时还是寡居之身，就是阿伦德尔太太，后来又嫁了人，如今远在印度。同去的还有一位年轻人，是阿伦德尔太太的朋友。

至于同罗杰·格雷托雷克斯一同出门，那也只是偶一为之，而且自他吐露爱情之后便再没有过。没有，自打丈夫抱病，就再没有过。

艾薇·莱克斯顿暗地里多么庆幸，近来她同罗杰·格雷托雷克斯实在是少见面啊！

===Original===

And then, all at once, the inspector had said something which had made, as she put it to herself, her heart stand still.

“I suppose there is a surgery attached to the house in Ferry Place?” he had remarked, speaking his thought aloud. And it had been those words, as to the probable existence of a surgery, that had suddenly made up her mind for her as to the line she would take concerning Roger Gretorex and their relations to one another.

Did Ivy Lexton then realise the full import of what she had done? Most certainly not. Subconsciously she was aware that her avowals, her timorous admissions as to his passion for her lovely self, could do Gretorex no good. But her only object had been to shift, at any cost, suspicion from herself.

===Chinese===

接着，探长冷不丁说了一句什么话，用她自己的话说，那叫她的心都停摆了。

「我猜，费里街那所宅子里附设着一间诊室吧？」他当时这样说，是有意把心里想的说出声来。而正是关于可能存在一间诊室的这几句话，蓦地替她拿定了主意：在罗杰·格雷托雷克斯以及两人关系这桩事上，她该取怎样的口径。

那么，艾薇·莱克斯顿当时可曾意识到自己这一手全部的分量？千真万确没有。她只是下意识地明白：她的那些招供，她对他倾心于自己这可爱人儿的怯生生的招认，于格雷托雷克斯没有半点好处。可她唯一图的就是，不惜任何代价，把嫌疑从自己身上挪开。

===Original===

There had been an interval, perhaps as long as a quarter of an hour, when she had been alone with Inspector Orpington. He had sent away his sergeant into the dining-room next door. At the time she had not known why.

Then he had spoken to her kindly, yet in a very solemn, searching tone, adjuring her to be frank, and to tell him of any knowledge, or even suspicion, she might harbour in her mind. But though every word he said added to her secret terror, that terror which was at once tangible and vague, for she knew nothing of the law, she had been wary, and very clever, in her answers.

Then the other man had come back, and to her surprise and horror she found she was expected to sign a statement.

===Chinese===

中间有一段空当，约莫有一刻钟之久，只她一个人同奥平顿探长待着。他把警佐打发到隔壁的饭厅里去了。当时她并不知是为了什么。

随后他好言好语地对她说起话来，语气却极其郑重、极具穿透之力，恳切叮嘱她放坦白些，心里存着什么知情、甚至仅只是疑心，都讲给他听。可是尽管他说的每一个字都在加深她心底那份恐怖——那份既真切又茫然的恐怖，因为她对法律一无所知——她回答起来却步步留神，而且机巧得很。

后来那名警佐回来了，她又惊又怕地发现，人家竟要她在一纸供词上签字画押。

===Original===

“You had better read it over,” the inspector had said quietly.

And Ivy had read it over, hardly realising what it was she was reading, but having the wit to know that this man from Scotland Yard had played fair by her.

Though in a sense Ivy had said nothing of any importance, apart from that fatal admission of Roger Gretorex’s love for her, Orpington had left the flat well satisfied. He believed he now had the threads of what had seemed at first such a mystery, all clutched up into his hands. Incidentally, at the end of that long searching inquisition he had reconstituted every moment of the last day of the dead man’s life.

===Chinese===

「你最好从头看一遍。」探长平静地说。

艾薇便从头看了一遍，几乎不知自己读的是些什么，却还存着那份心智，晓得这个苏格兰场来的人待她行事公道。

虽说在某种意义上，除了招认罗杰·格雷托雷克斯爱她那致命的一笔，艾薇并没有说出什么要紧的东西，奥平顿却是心满意足地离开了公寓。他相信，这桩起初看似重重谜团的案子，如今线头已尽数攥在他掌心里了。附带一提，在那场冗长而细密的盘问收尾时，他已把死者生命最后一天的每一时刻都重新拼合了出来。

===Original===

But as he had got up to leave, he had suddenly returned to the fact that Mrs. Lexton had been with her husband alone on that last afternoon of his life. That the inspector evidently attached importance to this fact brought back all Ivy’s terrors. But her remarkable powers of dissimulation stood her in good stead, and she again gave a pathetic little account of those few last minutes with her husband.

“I don’t quite know how long it was. Time went by quickly, for I thought he was better; and we had a little chat, making up our minds where we would go when he was well enough to have a change.”

“Did he have anything to drink﻿—a dose of medicine while you were with him, Mrs. Lexton?”

“Oh, no! Nurse was not out long enough for that.”

===Chinese===

可就在他起身告辞的当口，又突然绕回到那桩事实上：丈夫生命的最后一个下午，莱克斯顿太太曾同他单独在一起。探长显然对此节甚为看重，这一来，艾薇的种种恐怖又一齐涌了回来。但她那身出色的装假本领正帮上了忙，于是她把同丈夫最后相处的那几分钟，又凄楚动人地小小铺叙了一遍。

「我说不太准待了多久。时间过得快，因为我觉得他见好；我们闲聊了一阵，商量定等他好利索了、可以换换空气的时候，我们到哪儿去。」

「他可吃过喝过什么——你陪着的时候，服过一剂药吗，莱克斯顿太太？」

「哦，没有！护士出去的时间没有那么长。」

===Original===

And then it was that Orpington made his one mistake. Not that it made any difference at all in the long run. But, unconsciously, he had been affected by Ivy’s soft, feminine charm, as well as by her fragile loveliness.

“Of course we shall let you know the result of our investigations as soon as I have concluded my inquiries. As for Dr. Gretorex, I shall go and see him as soon as I have had something to eat.”

See Roger? Roger, who was so truthful, so incapable of telling even a white lie?

===Chinese===

也正是在这儿，奥平顿犯了他唯一的一处错。虽说从长远看这丝毫无关大局，可他不知不觉间已被艾薇那温软的女人风韵，连同她那娇弱动人的姿色打动了。

「等我的查问一了，我们自会把侦查结果奉告。至于格雷托雷克斯医生，我吃过点东西就马上去见他。」

去见罗杰？罗杰，那个如此真诚无欺、连一句善意的谎话都不会说的人？

===Original===

The knowledge that Gretorex was going to be subjected to a searching examination made her turn faint with fear. Had she been guilty of folly, and worse than folly, in admitting, nay, in volunteering the information, that Roger loved her? Within herself she debated that question. If Orpington had suspected the truth, then, of course, she had been wise. But had he﻿—had he?

In any case, the warning of Gretorex had seemed the only thing to do, and on the whole she now felt strengthened, comforted, by his unquestioning faith.

===Chinese===

一想到格雷托雷克斯将要去受一场细密的盘诘，她便怕得几乎发晕。她承认——不，是她主动供出——罗杰爱她这件事，是不是干了件蠢事，甚且是比蠢事更糟的事？她在心里反复掂量这个问题。倘若奥平顿起先就疑到了真相，那她自然是聪明的。可他疑了吗——他疑了吗？

无论如何，给格雷托雷克斯报个信在当时似乎是唯一可做的事，而总的看来，此刻想到他那毫不置疑的信任，她便觉得腰杆又硬了些，心里也得了些安慰。

===Original===

As at last she went up in the lift of the Duke of Kent Mansion, she heard quick, light steps running up the stairs, and she shivered with apprehension. But it was only a telegraph boy, and as they arrived together at the door of the flat, she asked quickly, “Is the name Lexton?”

“Yes, ma’am, ‘Mrs. Lexton,’ ” and he handed her the telegram.

In her bedroom she tore open the buff envelope and looked first at the signature:

> Just read with great concern result postmortem. Have cabled John Oram solicitor instructing him to afford you all help financial and other on my behalf. Sister dangerously ill or would return at once myself. Please keep me acquainted with any developments. Deepest sympathy.

> Miles Rushworth.

===Chinese===

末了她乘着肯特公爵大厦的电梯上楼时，听得楼梯上有轻快急促的脚步声一路奔上来，不由得心头疑惧，打了个寒噤。原来只是个电报投递员；两人一道到了公寓门口，她急急地问：「是不是莱克斯顿家的？」

「是的，太太，是『莱克斯顿太太』收。」说着他把电报递给了她。

她回到卧室，撕开牛皮纸信封，头一眼先落在署名上：

> 惊悉验尸结果，甚为挂念。已去电律师约翰·奥拉姆，嘱其代表我向你提供财务及其他一切援助。胞妹病危，否则我必即刻亲自返回。事态有任何进展，盼随时见告。谨致最深切的慰问。

> 迈尔斯·拉什沃思。

===Original===

She burst into hysterical tears of relief. Nothing, surely, could happen to her now that Rushworth had come to her help?

Always Ivy Lexton had been sheltered, guarded, lifted over the rough places of life by men﻿—men who had been conquered, pressed into her service, by that alluring quality which means so much more than beauty. But not one of the many men to whom she had cause to be grateful in her life had been in the powerful position of Miles Rushworth, as regarded either character or wealth.

She lay down on the comfortable couch which stood at right angles to her pretty bed. It was the first time she had ever done so, for she was a strong young woman, in spite of the air of delicacy which added so much to her charm in the eyes of many people, women as well as men. But she felt tired﻿—dreadfully, consciously tired, today.

===Chinese===

她一下子迸出了歇斯底里的宽慰之泪。拉什沃思既已赶来援手，如今她肯定再不会出什么事了吧？

艾薇·莱克斯顿这一生，从来都有男人为她遮蔽风雨、护持左右，把她高高举过人生的坎坷之处——这些男人无不被她征服，收来为她效劳，凭的是那种比美貌要紧得多的迷人禀赋。可在她此生有理由感激的那许多男人当中，论品性也罢，论财富也罢，没有一个及得上迈尔斯·拉什沃思这般位高势重。

她在那张舒适的躺椅上卧了下来——躺椅同她那张漂亮的床摆成直角。这是她生平头一回这样躺下，因为她本是个强健的少妇，尽管那副娇怯怯的神态，无论在男人还是许多女人眼里，都大大增添了她的魅力。可今天她实在累了——累得骇人，累得清清楚楚。

===Original===

She closed her eyes and, deliberately, she lived over again Rushworth’s last long, passionate embrace on the yacht. It was as if she heard spoken aloud his ardent, brokenhearted words, “*If only you were free!*”

Well? She was free now, even at what she was beginning to realise might be a fearful cost. Even so, it could only be a question of months, perhaps of weeks, before she became Rushworth’s wife.

She opened her eyes and smiled for the first time that day, a radiant, secretly confident smile.

===Chinese===

她阖上双眼，存心重温起游艇上拉什沃思那最后一个悠长而炽烈的拥抱。恍然间，她又听见他脱口喊出那句炽烈而伤心的痴语：「*你要是自由之身，该有多好！*」

怎么样？如今她自由了，纵使她开始意识到，这代价也许骇人。即便如此，她之成为拉什沃思的妻子，也不过是几个月、也许几个星期之内的事了。

她睁开眼睛，当天头一回绽出了笑容——一脸光彩照人、暗自笃定的笑。

===Original===

Leaping off the couch she read through Rushworth’s long telegram again.

John Oram? What a curious name. Being a solicitor, he must be on the telephone. She would ring him up immediately after she had had lunch, and ask him to come and see her.

Going into the hall, she called out, in almost a joyous voice, “Nurse? Here I am! I’m so sorry to have kept you waiting.”

===Chinese===

她从躺椅上一跃而起，把拉什沃思那封长电报又通读了一遍。

约翰·奥拉姆？好古怪的名字。他既是位律师，想必装着电话。午饭后她要立刻打给他，请他来见她。

她走进门厅，用近乎欢快的声音喊道：「护士？我来了！真对不起，让您久等了。」

===Original===

Nurse Bradfield came slowly down the corridor. The events of the last few days had aged her, and Ivy was struck by her sad, bewildered expression. She secretly wondered why Nurse Bradfield looked so old and unlike herself? Nurse Bradfield, lucky woman, had nothing to be afraid of.

Ivy Lexton never knew all she owed to the woman who had tended Jervis Lexton through his last illness. When Inspector Orpington had done with the nurse, he had formed, albeit unconsciously, a very definite view of the dead man’s widow. Thus, even before he had seen Ivy, he had accepted Nurse Bradfield’s view of young Mrs. Lexton’s nature and way of life. That is, he regarded her as selfish, feather-headed, and extravagant; but good-natured, easygoing, and quite incapable of planning and of executing such a crime as that which had brought about her husband’s death.

That Jervis Lexton had died as the result of a foul crime on the part of some man or woman who had a strong motive for wishing him to be obliterated had appeared plain to the man in charge of the case, even before he had interviewed any of the people concerned with it.

===Chinese===

布拉德菲尔德护士从走廊那头缓缓走来。这几日的变故使她见老，她那哀伤迷惘的神情叫艾薇吃了一惊。她暗自纳罕，布拉德菲尔德护士怎么显得这般苍老、这般不像平日的她了？布拉德菲尔德护士是个有福气的女人，没什么可怕的。

艾薇·莱克斯顿始终不曾知道，自己欠了这位看护着杰维斯·莱克斯顿走完末一场病的女人多少。奥平顿探长问完护士之后，虽说是无心的，却已对死者的遗孀形成了一个非常明确的看法。这样，他还没见到艾薇，就已经接受了布拉德菲尔德护士对年轻的莱克斯顿太太其人其生活方式的观感。那就是：他把这女人看作自私、头脑空空、挥霍无度；然而心肠不坏、脾气随和，而且断然筹划不出、也施行不了那样一桩断送她丈夫性命的罪案。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿死于某个男人或某个女人之手——那是桩卑劣的罪行，而下手的人必怀着巴不得他从此消失的强烈动机——这一点，在主持此案的人尚未讯问任何涉案人等之前，就已经显得一清二楚。

===Original===

But if Ivy, unknowingly, had reason to be grateful to Nurse Bradfield, Nurse Bradfield just now had cause to be very grateful to Ivy. It meant a great deal to her that she could stay on here, in this luxurious flat, living in quietude and comfort, instead of going back to the hostel which was her only “home” between her cases.

She had already learnt, and great was her dismay thereat, that she would become an important witness for the Crown, should the mystery be so far cleared up as to bring about a trial for murder. Small wonder that today she felt too upset and too disturbed to eat, and she watched, with surprise, Ivy’s evident enjoyment of the good luncheon put before them.

The nurse was the more secretly astonished at the newly made widow’s look of cheerfulness because she was well aware that “little Mrs. Lexton” was most uncomfortably short of money.

===Chinese===

不过，如果说艾薇在不知不求之间有理由感激布拉德菲尔德护士，布拉德菲尔德护士眼下也有大大的理由感激艾薇。能继续留在这套豪华公寓里，过几天安静舒适的日子，而不必回到那所寄宿舍去——两桩看护差事之间，那是她唯一可称「家」的地方——这对她实在非同小可。

她已经得知——而且为此惊惶不已——倘若这桩疑案能澄清到提起谋杀审判的地步，她将成为控方的重要证人（witness for the Crown）。

这位新居孀的妇人一脸快活气色，护士愈觉暗暗称奇，因为她深知「小莱克斯顿太太」手头拮据，拮据得够呛。

===Original===

All that morning, and especially during the latter half of that morning, there had come a procession of tradespeople to the flat requesting immediate payment of their accounts. Some of them had been interviewed by the cook, others by Nurse Bradfield herself. As for Ivy, she had absolutely refused to see any of them. “I have no money at all just now,” she had observed sadly. “But of course everybody will be paid in time.”

Nurse Bradfield had even begun to wonder if she would ever be repaid a certain ten pounds which she had lent Mrs. Lexton a few days before. But she was not as much troubled by that thought as some of Ivy Lexton’s fairly well-to-do friends might have been. She even told herself that, after all, she was now receiving far more than ten pounds’ worth of comfort and quiet.

As if something of what she was thinking flashed from the nurse’s mind to hers, Ivy said suddenly, “I shall have plenty of money soon, Nurse. And the moment I’ve got anything I’ll give you back that money you were kind enough to lend me.”

===Chinese===

那天一整个上午，尤其是临近晌午那阵子，讨账的商家走马灯似的登门，要求立即清偿欠款。有的由厨娘出面应付了过去，有的则由布拉德菲尔德护士亲自接待。至于艾薇，他们中间随便哪一个，她都断然拒不接见。「我眼下当真是一文不名，」她曾黯然说道，「不过到时候，每一笔账自然都会付清。」

前几天她借给莱克斯顿太太的那十英镑，到底还要不要得回来，布拉德菲尔德护士甚至已经开始犯嘀咕了。不过这一点心事，还不至于像艾薇·莱克斯顿那些家境小康的朋友似的叫她烦恼。她甚至对自己说，不管怎样，她如今享着的这份安逸清静，早抵得过十个英镑了。

仿佛护士心里正想的有几分闪进了她的脑子，艾薇突然开口：「我不久就会有大把的钱了，护士。手头一有进项，我马上就把您好心借给我的那笔钱还您。」

===Original===

There was a tone of real sincerity in her voice, and Nurse Bradfield felt reassured.

“I only want it back,” she said quietly, “when you can really give it me conveniently, Mrs. Lexton. Of course ten pounds is a good deal of money to me. But now that I know poor Mr. Lexton was not insured, I realise that things must be very difficult for you.”

“It’s going to be quite all right,” exclaimed Ivy impulsively.

Oh! what a difference to life Rushworth’s cable had made! She felt almost hysterical with joy and relief.

===Chinese===

她话音里透着实打实的诚恳，布拉德菲尔德护士宽了心。

「这笔钱我只求，」她平静地说，「等你真正方便的时候再还我，莱克斯顿太太。十英镑在我自然不是小数目。不过如今我知道可怜的莱克斯顿先生没有投人寿险，也就明白您眼下必定十分艰难了。」

「一切都会好起来的！」艾薇脱口喊道。

啊！拉什沃思这封电报，给生活带来了多大的改变啊！她心里那份欢喜与宽慰，几乎又要化作歇斯底里。

===Original===

And then, as there came a ring at the bell, she said quickly to the maid who was waiting at table, “Do tell whoever it is that I shall be able to pay up everything soon﻿—I hope even within the next few days.”

But this time the visitor was not an anxious tradesman. He was a tall, thin, elderly man, with a keen, shrewd face, who gave his name as “Mr. Oram.”

After a few moments spent by him in the hall, he was shown into the drawing-room, there to wait for the lady concerning whom he already felt a keen curiosity.

===Chinese===

正说着，门铃响了，她急忙对侍立在桌旁布菜的女仆说：「不管来的是哪一位，务必告诉他，我不久就能把账全都付清——我希望就在这几天之内。」

可这一回登门的并不是心焦的讨账商人。来客是个瘦高个子的上了年纪的男人，生着一张精明锐利的面孔，自报姓名是「奥拉姆先生」。

他在门厅里耽搁了片刻，便被引进客厅，在那里等候那位女主人——他早已对她起了强烈的好奇心。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===

John Oram was an old-fashioned solicitor of very high standing. His firm had always managed all the private business of the Rushworth family, and he was a personal friend of the client from whom he had received a long and explicit cable about two hours ago. The receipt of that cable, and above all the way it had been worded, had induced Mr. Oram to come himself to Duke of Kent Mansion, instead of sending one of his clerks. He felt intensely curious to see this newly made widow in whom Miles Rushworth evidently took so intimate and anxious an interest.

Rushworth’s cable to John Oram had been nearly three times the length of his cable to Ivy; and the purport of it had been that the solicitor was to help Mrs. Lexton in every way in his power. The last words of the cable had run: “Find out from Mrs. Lexton the name of her bankers, and place two thousand pounds to her credit.”

After reading the cable, Mr. Oram had sent for his head clerk, an acute, clever man named Alfred Finch, who was some twenty years younger than himself.

“Can you tell me anything of some people of the name of Lexton, who live in Duke of Kent Mansion? I gather there’s some sort of legal trouble afoot.”

===Chinese===

约翰·奥拉姆是个恪守旧派作风、声望极高的律师。他的事务所一向代管着拉什沃思家的全部私人事务，而两小时前给他发来一封长而详明的电报的那位委托人，更是他私交甚笃的朋友。正是收到了这封电报，尤其是那措辞，促使奥拉姆先生亲自来到肯特公爵大厦，而不是打发一名书记员跑腿。他怀着极浓的好奇心，想见一见这位新寡的妇人——迈尔斯·拉什沃思对她显然怀着如此亲昵而焦灼的关切。

拉什沃思发给约翰·奥拉姆的电报，差不多三倍于发给艾薇的那封；要旨便是：这位律师须竭尽所能，从方方面面帮助莱克斯顿太太。电报的末尾一句写道：“向莱克斯顿太太问明她开户银行的字号，将两千英镑存入她的名下。”

读过电报，奥拉姆先生便叫人请来他的主任书记员——一个精明干练、名叫阿尔弗雷德·芬奇（Alfred Finch）的人，比自己年轻二十岁上下。

“你可知道住在肯特公爵大厦的姓莱克斯顿的那户人家的底细？我听说他们眼下正有什么官司缠身。”

===Original===

The answer had been immediate, and had filled him with both surprise and dismay.

“Yes, sir, I know all there is to be known. It’s not very much, yet. A Mr. Jervis Lexton died some days ago in one of the Duke of Kent Mansion flats. And, as the result of a postmortem, it has been discovered that death was occasioned through the administration of a large dose of arsenic.”

The speaker waited a moment. His curiosity was considerably whetted, for he had seen a look of astonishment, almost of horror, come over his employer’s usually impassive face.

Alfred Finch went on, speaking in a more serious tone:

“This Mr. Jervis Lexton must have been a man of means, for you may remember, sir, that he drew up the lease of a flat in Duke of Kent Mansion for the Misses Rushworth about eighteen months ago.”

===Chinese===

回答脱口而出，却让他又惊又忧。

“知道，先生，凡有底可查的我全知道。眼下知道得还不多。几天前，一位杰维斯·莱克斯顿先生在肯特公爵大厦的一套公寓里死了。而且，验尸的结果已经查明，死因是被人施用了一大剂砒霜。”

说这话的人顿了片刻。他的好奇心被大大吊了起来，因为他看见雇主那张素来不动声色的脸上，掠过一丝惊愕、几乎是骇然的表情。

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇接着往下说，语气愈发郑重：

“这位杰维斯·莱克斯顿先生想必是个有钱人，因为您也许记得，先生，大约十八个月前，他曾为拉什沃思小姐们（the Misses Rushworth）拟订过肯特公爵大厦一套公寓的租约。”

===Original===

“Aye, aye, I do remember that. I think the rent of their flat was four hundred a year, and the two ladies had to pay a considerable premium on going in. As you say, the Lextons must be well-to-do.”

Mr. Finch allowed himself to smile.

“There’s only one of them now, sir﻿—the dead man’s widow. She’s said to be very attractive, and well known in smart society.”

“I see. That will do.”

===Chinese===

“是啊，是啊，那我记得。我想她们那套公寓的年租金是四百镑，两位小姐进宅时还得付一笔可观的顶费。正如你所说，莱克斯顿家想必相当殷实。”

芬奇先生这才容许自己露出一丝笑意。

“如今只剩一位了，先生——就是死者的遗孀。据说她非常迷人，在时髦社交场里颇有名声。”

“明白了。就这样吧。”

===Original===

It was no wonder that John Oram, while waiting in the drawing-room of the flat for Ivy to join him, gazed about him with a good deal of interest. Then, all at once, he recognised a fine picture which he knew to be the property of the Misses Rushworth, his clients, and Miles Rushworth’s cousins. In a moment what had appeared a mystery was to his mind cleared up. There must be, there was, of course, some sort of connection between the Rushworths and the Lextons; and the rich, precise, old-fashioned maiden ladies, who, he now remembered, were wintering abroad, had lent their flat to these family connections.

That would explain everything﻿—Miles Rushworth’s urgent cable, as also his evident anxiety that everything should be done to help and succour Mrs. Lexton in her distress.

Just as he came to this satisfactory explanation of what had puzzled and disturbed him, the door of the drawing-room opened, and Ivy walked in.

She held out her little hand. “Mr. Oram?” she cried eagerly, “I’m so glad to see you! I had a cable just before lunch from Mr. Rushworth, telling me that you were going to help me. Everything is so dreadful, so extraordinary, that I feel utterly bewildered, as well as miserable﻿—” and then tears strangled her voice.

For a moment her visitor said nothing. He was amazed at her exceeding loveliness, puzzled also, for he was very observant, by the expression which now lit up the beautiful face before him. Though tears were running down her cheeks, it was such a happy expression.

===Chinese===

难怪约翰·奥拉姆在公寓客厅里等候艾薇出来相见时，怀着浓厚的兴味朝四下打量。忽然，他认出了一幅精美的画作——他知道，这是他的委托人、也是迈尔斯·拉什沃思两位表亲的拉什沃思小姐们的藏品。顷刻之间，先前看似蹊跷的事在他心里豁然开朗。拉什沃思家与莱克斯顿家之间必有、当然有某种渊源；而那两位有钱、刻板、守旧的老小姐——他如今想起来，正在国外过冬——是把自己的公寓借给了这几门亲戚。

这一下就全解释通了——迈尔斯·拉什沃思那封十万火急的电报，以及他那份显而易见的焦灼：务必想方设法周济危难中的莱克斯顿太太。

他刚把这个令他困惑不安的疑团解说妥帖，客厅的门便开了，艾薇走了进来。

她伸出那只小手。“是奥拉姆先生吧？”她热切地喊道，“见到您真是太好了！午饭前我收到拉什沃思先生的电报，说您会来帮我。这一切太可怕、太离奇了，我整个人都懵了，心里也难受极了——”说到这里，泪水哽住了她的嗓音。

来客一时没有作声。她的倾城之貌令他惊叹；而他素来眼尖，此刻她那张美丽的脸上焕发出的神情，更令他大惑不解。泪水明明正顺着她的双颊往下淌，那神情却是那样欢欣。

===Original===

“Won’t you sit down?”

Her tone was quite subdued now; the hysterical excitement which had been there had died out of her voice.

He obeyed her silently, and there shot over Ivy Lexton a quick feeling of misgiving. Mr. Oram looked so grave, so stern, and he was gazing at her with so curiously close a scrutiny.

“It’s very kind of you to have come so soon,” she said nervously.

“I am anxious to help you in every way possible, Mrs. Lexton,” he answered quietly.

Though the old solicitor was exceedingly impressed by Ivy’s beauty, instead of being attracted, he felt, if anything, slightly repelled, by her appearance.

===Chinese===

“您请坐，好吗？”

她的语调此刻已全然平静下来；先前那歇斯底里的激动，已从她的话音里褪尽了。

他默默地依言落座，而一阵不安倏地掠过艾薇·莱克斯顿的心头。奥拉姆先生的神色那么凝重、那么严峻，而且他正用一种出奇逼视的目光盯着她细瞧。

“您来得这么快，真是太费心了。”她局促不安地说。

“我切望竭尽一切可能帮助您，莱克斯顿太太。”他平静地答道。

老律师虽然对艾薇的美貌叹服不已，却非但没有为之所动，反倒——若说有什么感觉的话——对她的模样隐隐生出几分反感。

===Original===

For one thing, he was sufficiently old-fashioned to feel really surprised, and even shocked, by her “makeup.”

Ivy had made up more than usual this morning, and before coming into the drawing-room just now she had used her lipstick quite recklessly. So it was that while Mr. Oram asked her certain questions, each one of which was to the point, and allowed for but very little prevarication on her part, he avoided looking straight at her.

How astounding, he said to himself with dismay, that such a woman should be a friend of Miles Rushworth! A direct question had shown him that she had no knowledge of, or even a bowing acquaintance with, the Misses Rushworth.

At last he said rather coldly, “I take it you are in possession of very little money?”

“Very little,” she answered, almost in a whisper.

===Chinese===

其一，他的守旧足以使他对她脸上那层“脂粉”当真感到诧异，甚至骇然。

艾薇今早的妆比往常上得更浓，方才走进客厅之前，还无所顾忌地又抹了一通口红。于是，当奥拉姆先生向她提出若干问题时——每个问题都直切要害，几乎不容她有支吾搪塞的余地——他始终避免正眼瞧她。

他愀然自忖：这样的一个女人，竟是迈尔斯·拉什沃思的朋友，岂不骇人听闻！一句直截了当的问话已让他明白：她对拉什沃思小姐们一无所知，连点头之交也谈不上。

他终于相当冷淡地开了口：“我猜您手头没多少现钱吧？”

“少得很。”她答道，声音低得近乎耳语。

===Original===

“At the request of Mr. Miles Rushworth, I have a sum of money to place at your disposal. As a matter of fact, it is a considerable sum﻿—two thousand pounds. If you will tell me who are your bankers, I﻿—”

And then Ivy, keeping the joy she felt out of her voice, interrupted him:

“I have not got a banking account, Mr. Oram. I had one many years ago, before my husband lost all his money, but I have not had one for over three years. And oh! it’s been so inconvenient.”

A kinder look came into the lawyer’s grave face.

“In that case, Mrs. Lexton, I advise you to open an account at the local branch of the Birmingham Bank. It is close here, in Kensington High Street. Mr. Rushworth informed me in his cable that you would probably stay on in this flat for the next few weeks.”

“I should like to do that,” she said in a low tone.

===Chinese===

“受迈尔斯·拉什沃思先生之托，我有一笔钱要交由您支配。实不相瞒，这是相当大的一笔——两千英镑。您若肯告诉我您的开户银行是哪一家，我——”

这时艾薇打断了他，话音里听不出她心头那份狂喜：

“我在银行没有账户，奥拉姆先生。多年前倒是有一个，那是我丈夫把钱赔光之前的事了，可我已经三年多没有账户了。唉！真是不方便极了。”

律师那张凝重的脸上，浮起了一丝较为温和的神色。

“既然如此，莱克斯顿太太，我劝您在伯明翰银行（Birmingham Bank）的本地分行开个户。就在这附近，肯辛顿大街上。拉什沃思先生在电报里告知我，往后几个星期，您多半还要在这套公寓里住下去。”

“我很愿意。”她低声说。

===Original===

“Your husband, I understand, was a great friend of Mr. Rushworth?”

“Yes, my husband was working in Mr. Rushworth’s office when he fell ill.”

“Was he indeed?”

That the Lextons could be what the sender of the cable had called “my closest friends” had surprised the solicitor. He had believed himself acquainted with all Miles Rushworth’s intimate circle.

Ivy had come across a good many lawyers in her life, and she had always found them bright, cheery, and pleasant. All of them, to a man, had admired her, and made her feel that they did so.

Very, very different was this lawyer’s attitude. She realised that he did not approve of her, and she even suspected that he regretted his client’s interest in her. That was quite enough for Ivy, and she began to long intensely for Mr. Oram to go away. She had already made up her mind that he was “horrid,” and she was sorry indeed that such a man should be Miles Rushworth’s representative.

===Chinese===

“我听说，您丈夫是拉什沃思先生的一位至交？”

“是的，我丈夫病倒的时候，正在拉什沃思先生的办事处做事。”

“当真？”

莱克斯顿夫妇竟会是电报发报人口中“我最亲近的朋友们”，这让律师颇感意外。他原以为自己识遍了迈尔斯·拉什沃思亲近圈子里的每一个人。

艾薇这辈子打过交道的律师为数不少，他们个个都让她觉得开朗、快活、讨人喜欢。无一例外，他们全都很赏识她，而且让她感觉得到这份赏识。

这一位律师的态度却大大不然。她明白他不认可自己，甚至疑心他对委托人垂青于自己一事暗自惋惜。对艾薇来说，这就足够了，她开始一门心思巴望着奥拉姆先生快些走。她已经拿定主意，这人“讨人嫌”，也着实懊恼这样的人偏偏是迈尔斯·拉什沃思的代表。

===Original===

“I will pay in the cheque to the Birmingham Bank tomorrow morning, Mrs. Lexton,” said the solicitor. “I will call for you, if I may, at eleven, for you will have to come too, in order that the manager may register your signature.”

At last he got up, and then he said suddenly: “Have you yet seen anyone from the police?”

“Yes, I saw a gentleman from Scotland Yard this morning.”

“I trust your legal adviser was present.”

“I have no legal adviser,” and she looked at him surprised.

“I’m sorry for that. I had hoped to learn that you had a solicitor, and that he had been present. However, I don’t suppose it will make any odds. I presume you told the gentleman from Scotland Yard everything that it was within your power to tell him, concerning the mysterious circumstances surrounding Mr. Lexton’s death?”

===Chinese===

“明天上午我就把支票存进伯明翰银行，莱克斯顿太太。”律师说，“若蒙俯允，我十一点来接您，因为您也得同去一趟，好让经理登记您的签名。”

他终于站起身来，随后突然问道：“警方的人，您见过了吗？”

“见过了，今天上午来了一位苏格兰场的先生。”

“想必您的法律顾问当时在座吧。”

“我没有什么法律顾问。”她一脸诧异地望着他。

“那我深表遗憾。我原指望得知您有一位律师，而且他当时在座。不过，我想这也不碍什么事。想必您已经把您力所能及的一切都告诉那位苏格兰场的先生了——关于莱克斯顿先生死亡前后的种种神秘情形？”

===Original===

“Yes, I did,” she said falteringly.

It seemed to her that he was looking at her with such a hard, cold look on his bloodless face. She even had a queer feeling that this Mr. Oram could see right through her, and she felt a touch of deadly terror.

But Ivy’s fears were quite unfounded. The solicitor’s view of Ivy Lexton was very much what “the gentleman from Scotland Yard’s” had been. But whereas Inspector Orpington had liked and pitied her, Rushworth’s lawyer already regretted that, if only as a matter of common humanity, he must now secure for her the best legal advice in his power.

John Oram had the faults of his qualities. His life’s work had brought him in contact with more than one skilful adventuress. But against such a woman, when she came across his path, the dice were already loaded.

Thus he had never had much trouble with the kind of girl who infatuates a foolish “elder son,” and then, maybe, tries to extract an enormous sum out of him by a threat of a breach of promise case. More difficult to deal with he had found, in his long career as a family solicitor, the sort of woman blackmailer who has letters in her possession. But, even in regard to that type of woman, Mr. Oram, with the law on his side, invariably came out of the duel triumphant.

He had never had to do, even remotely, with a case of murder, and the last thing that would have occurred to his mind was that this lovely young fribble of a woman﻿—for such was his old-fashioned expression﻿—could be a secret poisoner.

===Chinese===

“是的，都说了。”她结结巴巴地说。

在她看来，他正隔着那张血色全无的脸，用一种冷硬无比的目光望着她。她甚至生出一种怪异的感觉：这位奥拉姆先生竟能把她从里到外看个通透。她感到一阵死一般的恐惧。

但艾薇的恐惧全然是无端的。这位律师对艾薇·莱克斯顿的看法，与那位“苏格兰场的先生”的看法大同小异。只是奥平顿探长对她还存着喜欢与怜惜，而拉什沃思的这位律师却已经在暗自懊悔：哪怕单凭一点普通的人道之情，他如今也必须为她谋取他力所能及的最好的法律意见。

约翰·奥拉姆有其优点，亦有其与优点相伴的缺憾。他毕生的执业使他接触过一个又一个手段高明的女冒险家。可这样的女人一旦撞到他手里，骰子便早已灌了铅。

因此，他从不曾为那一类姑娘费过多少心——那种迷住一个愚蠢的“长子”、继而或许以毁弃婚约之诉相要挟、想从他身上榨出一大笔钱的姑娘。在他漫长的家事律师生涯中，他觉得更难对付的，是手里握着书信的那一类女敲诈者。但即便是面对那一类女人，奥拉姆先生有法律作后盾，每场交锋总能凯旋。

他从未沾手过谋杀案，连沾一点边也不曾有过；而他万万不会想到的是，这个漂亮而轻浮浅薄的年轻女人——他用的是他那老派的字眼——竟可能是个暗地下毒的行家。

===Original===

“I think you must authorise me to instruct counsel to represent you at the inquest which I understand is about to be held.”

“What is counsel?” asked Ivy.

She felt surprised and uneasy. Was this disagreeable old man going to run up what she knew was called “a lawyer’s bill” which she would have to pay out of Rushworth’s munificent gift?

Mr. Oram looked at her with scarce concealed contempt.

“A counsel,” he replied drily, “is any member of the Bar. But naturally some are better than others, and, with your permission, I will obtain for you the services of a gentleman who is thoroughly experienced in cases of this kind.”

===Chinese===

“我想，您必须授权我延请一位出庭律师（counsel），在据我所知即将开庭的死因研讯（inquest）上代表您。”

“什么是出庭律师？”艾薇问。

她又惊又不安。这个讨厌的老头子，是不是要攒出一笔她知道名叫“律师账单”的开销，到头来还得从拉什沃思先生慷慨的馈赠里支付？

奥拉姆先生望着她，几乎不加掩饰地露出轻蔑。

“出庭律师，”他干巴巴地答道，“就是出庭律师界（the Bar）里的任何一位。不过自然有高下之分，如您俯允，我将为您延请一位在此类案件上阅历烂熟的先生。”

---

===Original===

The next morning Mr. Oram arrived at his office early, and, after glancing over his letters, he had just made out a cheque for two thousand pounds to “the order of Mrs. Ivy Lexton,” when a card was brought into his private room. But before he looked at the card he had already fully made up his mind that he could see no one, however important their business might be, till his return from Kensington.

Already the solicitor and his head clerk, Alfred Finch, had gone into the question of who should represent Mrs. Lexton at the inquest, and at the various other proceedings which were likely to take place in connection with Jervis Lexton’s mysterious death. Money, as the saying is, being no object, they had selected as her counsel one known to them to be by far the soundest man for that sort of watching brief.

The old lawyer was sorry indeed that Miles Rushworth had brought him in touch with what he termed to himself “this very unpleasant business.”

His feeling was not shared by his head clerk. Alfred Finch was already keenly interested in the Lexton case. He was an intelligent man, keen about his work whatever it might be, and he already had managed to make certain pertinent inquiries. Indeed, he very much startled Mr. Oram by a remark he made towards the end of their discussion.

“They do say, sir, that Scotland Yard as good as know already who poisoned Mr. Lexton. I think it quite probable that you will see the news of an arrest on the newspaper placards on your way to Duke of Kent Mansion.”

===Chinese===

第二天一早，奥拉姆先生便到了事务所，翻过信件之后，刚开出一张两千英镑的支票，收款人为“艾薇·莱克斯顿太太”，一张名片就送进了他的私室。可还没去看那名片，他已完全拿定主意：从肯辛顿回来之前，无论来人的事务多么紧要，他一概不见。

律师同他的主任书记员阿尔弗雷德·芬奇，早已商讨过由谁代表莱克斯顿太太出席死因研讯，以及围绕杰维斯·莱克斯顿离奇之死可能陆续进行的其他种种程序。俗话说得好，钱不成问题，他们已为她选定了一位出庭律师——据他们所知，办那类旁听代理（watching brief）的案子，再没有比他更稳妥的人选。

老律师着实懊恼，迈尔斯·拉什沃思竟让他同这桩他私下称作“极不痛快的事务”的案子沾上了关系。

他的主任书记员却全无此感。阿尔弗雷德·芬奇已经对莱克斯顿案兴味浓烈。他是个聪明人，无论手头是什么差事都兴致勃勃，而且已经悄悄做了几番切中要害的查访。果然，在两人商讨临近尾声时，他的一句话让奥拉姆先生大吃一惊。

“外面确实都在说，先生，苏格兰场差不多已经知道是谁毒死了莱克斯顿先生。我看十有八九，您去肯特公爵大厦的路上，就能在报纸招贴上看到逮捕落网的消息了。”

===Original===

“What sort of person has been, or is to be, arrested, Finch? Have you discovered that?”

“Well, sir, I haven’t yet got hold of the man’s name. But I gather he’s a gentleman, and one who was described to me as﻿—” he coughed discreetly “﻿—a beau of Mrs. Lexton. Mrs. Lexton seems to have been a bit of a flyer, sir. She was out every night dancing at what they call a smart nightclub, or in some big hotel, during the days when her unfortunate husband was being slowly done to death by this friend of hers.”

“Have you heard anything serious against Mrs. Lexton’s character?”

Mr. Oram was very old-fashioned. The term “nightclub” signified to him something vaguely terrible, and utterly disreputable.

“Oh, no, sir, there’s nothing against her. On the contrary, the story goes that, though the man under suspicion was crazy about her, she only flirted with him, so to speak. Mrs. Lexton, it seems, gave him away, quite unknowingly, to the C.I.D. inspector who is in charge of the case.”

Finch smiled, “They say it’s likely to be the most important case of the kind there’s been at the Old Bailey for many a long day. The public are about ready for another murder mystery.”

===Chinese===

“已经被捕、或将遭逮捕的，是个什么样的人，芬奇？这个你打听到了吗？”

“呃，先生，那人的名字我还没弄到手。不过我听说他是个上流人士，人家向我描述他时说——”他审慎地咳了一声——“说是莱克斯顿太太的一位情郎。莱克斯顿太太瞧着是个风流轻佻的人物呢，先生。她那不幸的丈夫被这位朋友慢慢毒死的那些日子里，她夜夜都在外头，泡在他们所谓的时髦夜总会里，要么就在哪家大饭店里跳舞。”

“关于莱克斯顿太太的名声，你听到过什么确凿的不利之辞吗？”

奥拉姆先生非常守旧。“夜总会”三个字，在他心里意味着某种说不清道不明的可怕去处，全然有失体统。

“哦，没有，先生，没有她什么把柄。恰恰相反，风闻是说，那个涉嫌的男人为她神魂颠倒，而她呢，可以说不过同他调调情罢了。莱克斯顿太太似乎是在全然不自觉的情形下，把他的底细捅给了主办此案的那位刑事调查部探长。”

芬奇笑了：“他们说，这多半会是很久以来老贝利（Old Bailey）所审理的最重大的一桩同类案子。公众也正巴望着再来一桩谋杀疑案呢。”

===Original===

“Not much mystery about it, if your information is correct, Finch,” observed Mr. Oram grimly.

“It’s Mrs. Lexton﻿—they say she is such a very pretty, smart little lady﻿—who will provide the mystery and the sensation, sir. She’ll be the principal witness for the Crown.”

Mr. Oram felt very much disturbed on hearing this piece of information.

“I do not regard myself as being in any sense Mrs. Lexton’s legal representative,” he said stiffly. “With regard to this lady, I am simply acting as Mr. Miles Rushworth’s solicitor.”

And now, just as he was reaching out for his hat and coat, feeling more perturbed than he would have cared to acknowledge, a client for whom he had a great regard called to see him. Though John Oram was not the kind of man who changes his mind lightly when he saw whose name was engraved on the card which had been brought in to him, the lawyer at once made up his mind that he must spare time for this visitor.

===Chinese===

“要是你的消息不差，芬奇，这案子可没什么离奇可言。”奥拉姆先生冷冷地说。

“离奇就离奇在莱克斯顿太太身上，先生——都说她是位非常标致时髦的小个子太太——是她要给这案子添上离奇和轰动。她将出任控方（the Crown，即检察机关）的主要证人。”

听了这番话，奥拉姆先生心绪大为不宁。

“无论从哪种意义上，我都不把自己看作莱克斯顿太太的法律代理人。”他生硬地说，“就这位太太而言，我不过是作为迈尔斯·拉什沃思先生的律师行事。”

正在这时，他伸手去取帽子和外套，心里的烦乱远比他愿意承认的更深，一位他素来极为敬重的委托人登门求见。约翰·奥拉姆虽不是那种轻易改主意的人，可一看递进来的名片上镌着谁的名字，律师当即拿定主意：这位来客，务必抽空一见。

===Original===

For one thing, her business must be serious, for she lived in the country, and this was the first time she had ever called on him without first making an appointment. Further, she was a widowed lady he had known since he was quite a young man, and for whom he had a very high esteem, and, it might almost be said, affection. But he had despised her husband, and he did not really like her son﻿—though the son had none of the faults which had brought his father to ruin. Lastly, Mr. Oram was willing to see this client because she was a woman of few words. She would tell him at once why she wished to see him, and then she would go away.

“Show Mrs. Gretorex in,” he said quickly.

“There is a young lady with her, sir.”

“A young lady?”

Did that mean that Roger Gretorex was thinking of getting married? If so, unless the girl had money, he would be doing a very foolish and improvident thing. Mr. Oram did not really think that this was at all likely to be the reason for Mrs. Gretorex’s unexpected visit; but a solicitor is apt to consider every possibility.

===Chinese===

一来，她的事想必非同小可，因为她住在乡下，而从不曾有过一回未经预约便登门求见的事。再者，她是一位他自青年时代起便相识的寡居夫人，他对她极为敬重，甚至可以说怀有几分情谊。可他一向瞧不起她的丈夫，也实在不喜欢她的儿子——尽管儿子身上并无把父亲拖向身败名裂的那些毛病。末了还有一层，奥拉姆先生愿意见这位委托人，是因为她是个言语简省的女人。她会立刻道明来意，说完便走。

“请格雷托雷克斯太太进来。”他急忙说。

“她还带着一位年轻小姐，先生。”

“年轻小姐？”

这是说，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯在打算结婚了吗？若是这样，除非那姑娘有钱，否则他干的便是一桩愚蠢而毫无远虑的事。奥拉姆先生倒并不真认为这会是格雷托雷克斯太太突然造访的缘由；不过，律师这种人，惯于把每一种可能都掂量一番。

===Original===

However, Mr. Oram’s old friend and client came unaccompanied through the baize door of his private room. Mrs. Gretorex had left Enid in the waiting-room, for there were certain things which she knew she would have to say, and which she felt she could only say when alone with the solicitor.

As she looked at her old lawyer’s stern face, though the expression on it was just a little softer than usual, even her high courage faltered.

“Perhaps you know, Mr. Oram,” she said in a low voice, “what it is that has brought me here this morning?”

“No,” he said, surprised, “I have no idea at all, Mrs. Gretorex﻿—”

He could see she was very much disturbed, and he drew forward a chair. After all, it wouldn’t hurt that frivolous little widow to wait for an extra half-hour or so for the two thousand pounds Miles Rushworth was so rashly presenting to her free and for nothing. Mr. Oram’s knowledge of human nature told him that probably a very great deal more money was coming Ivy’s way from the same source as this money came from.

===Chinese===

然而，独自穿过私室那扇呢绒门（baize door）进来的，只有奥拉姆先生的老朋友、老委托人。格雷托雷克斯太太把伊妮德留在了等候室里，因为有些话她知道自己非说不可，而她觉得，这些话只能同律师单独讲。

她望着老律师那张严峻的脸——虽然脸上的神情比平日略见柔和——纵然她素来刚毅，此刻也不禁气短。

“或许您已经知道了，奥拉姆先生，”她低声说，“今天早晨，是什么事把我引到这儿来的？”

“不知道，”他讶然答道，“我全然摸不着头脑，格雷托雷克斯太太——”

他看得出她心绪大乱，便向前拉过一把椅子。说到底，让那个轻浮的小寡妇为那两千英镑多等上半个钟头上下，又有何妨——迈尔斯·拉什沃思正大手笔地白送给她，分文不取。奥拉姆先生的人情练达告诉他：这笔钱从哪里来，日后多半还会有大得多的钱财，从同一个源头流进艾薇的腰包。

===Original===

Mrs. Gretorex bent a little forward. She fixed her sunken eyes, for she had not slept at all the night before, on the lawyer’s face.

“Roger has been arrested on the charge of having murdered a man called Jervis Lexton﻿—”

“Roger arrested on a charge of murder? God bless my soul!”

He took off his eyeglasses and began cleaning them mechanically with a small piece of wash-leather which he kept for that purpose.

Here was indeed a complication! And a very troublesome as well as a painful complication, from his point of view. His own connection with the Gretorex family was hereditary. His grandfather had been, not only the lawyer, but the very close friend and trustee, of Mrs. Gretorex’s father-in-law. As for the rich Rushworths, they were in Mr. Oram’s estimation mere upstarts compared to the ancient, if now impoverished, county family.

===Chinese===

格雷托雷克斯太太微微前倾，把那双深陷的眼睛——她前一夜根本不曾合眼——牢牢盯在律师的脸上。

“罗杰被捕了，罪名是谋害了一个叫杰维斯·莱克斯顿的人——”

“罗杰因谋杀罪名被捕？老天爷啊！”

他摘下眼镜，开始用一小块专门留着擦镜片用的麂皮，机械地擦拭起来。

这可真是节外生枝！而且在他看来，还是既棘手又痛心的一重枝节。他本人与格雷托雷克斯家的渊源是世代相传的。他的祖父不仅做过格雷托雷克斯太太公公的律师，还是这位老人家极亲近的朋友和受托人。至于有钱的拉什沃思家，在奥拉姆先生的估量里，同这门虽已败落却源远流长的郡中世家相比，不过是一伙暴发户罢了。

===Original===

“If you don’t mind,” he said suddenly, “I’ll send for Finch. In a case of this sort two heads are better than one. Also Finch can take notes of any information you can give me about the matter.”

Mrs. Gretorex would much rather have told her story to this good old friend alone. But she saw the sense of his suggestion, and they both waited in silence till the head clerk came in.

Mrs. Gretorex rose and shook hands with Mr. Finch. She was well acquainted with him, and she had always liked him.

“May I tell Finch what you have just told me?” Mr. Oram asked.

She bent her head, overwhelmed with a passion of agony and shame.

“Mrs. Gretorex has brought bad news, Finch. Her son has just been arrested on the charge of having caused the death, I presume by the administration of arsenic, of Mr. Jervis Lexton. I take it”﻿—and he looked very straight into the younger man’s face﻿—“that you had no notion of this fact, when you told me, this morning, that you had heard that an arrest was about to be effected in connection with the Lexton affair?”

===Chinese===

“您若不介意，”他突然说，“我去把芬奇叫来。这种案子，一人计短，二人计长。再者，凡您能就此事赐告的情况，也好让芬奇记录下来。”

格雷托雷克斯太太本宁愿单独向这位善良的老朋友倾诉原委。但她明白这话在理，于是两人默默等着主任书记员进来。

格雷托雷克斯太太起身与芬奇先生握手。她与他相熟已久，也一向喜欢他。

“您刚才对我讲的话，我可以告诉芬奇吗？”奥拉姆先生问。

她低下了头，被一阵锥心的痛苦与羞惭压倒了。

“格雷托雷克斯太太带来了坏消息，芬奇。她的儿子刚刚被捕，罪名是害死了杰维斯·莱克斯顿先生——我猜是施用砒霜所致。我这么说吧——”他直直地望进年轻人的眼睛，“今早你告诉我，你听说莱克斯顿案即将有人落网，那时候，你对这个情况毫无所知吧？”

===Original===

Alfred Finch prided himself on his self-control, and wise lack of emotion, where anything connected with business was concerned. But his face was full of dismay as he answered instantly, “No name at all was mentioned, sir. I was simply told that an arrest was imminent.”

He turned to Mrs. Gretorex. “When was it that Mr. Roger was arrested?” He had known “Mr. Roger” from childhood.

Tears welled up to her tired eyes. “Last evening,” she answered.

“I wish he’d sent for us at once,” Finch exclaimed. “It’s always important to get one’s blow in first, and especially over a matter of this kind.”

“My son was arrested at Anchorford House. I came up by the night train, as the police inspector from Lynchester said he would be brought to town the first thing this morning. I suppose he is in London by now.”

Finch looked at his employer.

===Chinese===

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇素来自负的，是凡涉公事时的自持，与明智的不动声色。可他应声作答时，脸上满是惊惶：“压根儿没提名字，先生。人家只告诉我，逮捕在即。”

他转向格雷托雷克斯太太。“罗杰先生是什么时候被捕的？”“罗杰先生”打小他就认识。

泪水涌上她疲惫的双眼。“昨天傍晚。”她答道。

“他要是一早就把我们找去就好了。”芬奇感叹道，“先下手为强，永远是紧要的，这种事上尤其如此。”

“我儿子是在安克福德府被捕的。我是坐夜车赶来的，因为林彻斯特来的探长说，今早一早他就会押到城里。我想他现在已经到伦敦了。”

芬奇看了看雇主。

===Original===

“In that case, don’t you think, sir, that I’d better go off at once and try to find Dr. Gretorex? Let me see. Where would he be charged?”

Mentally he answered his own question. Then he observed, “I hope he made no statement to the police?”

“He wished to make a statement, but the inspector advised him not to do so.”

“You think, Finch, that you’d better go off now, instantly?”

“I’m sure of it, sir. Even now, I fear I shall be too late to stop his saying something he’d best keep to himself.”

“I feel quite sure he has nothing to hide,” said Mrs. Gretorex rather stiffly. But neither of the two men made any comment on that.

===Chinese===

“那样的话，先生，您不觉得我最好立刻动身，去设法寻到格雷托雷克斯医生吗？让我想想。他会在哪儿过堂受审呢？”

他心里自问自答了这个问题，随后说道：“我希望他没向警方作什么口供吧？”

“他本想作一份口供，但探长劝他不要作。”

“你认为你最好现在就去，刻不容缓，芬奇？”

“我确信如此，先生。只怕就算现在赶去，也来不及拦住他说出那些最好烂在肚里的话了。”

“我深信他没有什么要隐瞒的。”格雷托雷克斯太太说，语气相当生硬。两个男人却谁也没有接口。

===Original===

Mr. Oram was the first to break the silence.

“Very well, Finch. You go off,” he said. “Start at once! And of course no expense is to be spared?”

He glanced at his client, and she quickly nodded.
