“Meanwhile, I’ll make rough notes of any information that Mrs. Gretorex is good enough to give me. But I don’t suppose she really knows very much.”

And then in a serious tone he asked her, “Were you yourself acquainted with Mr. and Mrs. Lexton?”

He put the question just as the other man was leaving the room, and Mrs. Gretorex saw Finch stay his steps. It was clear that he wished to hear her answer.

===Chinese===

还是奥拉姆先生先打破了沉默。

“很好，芬奇。你去吧。”他说，“即刻动身！当然，费用在所不惜，对吧？”

他望了一眼委托人，她迅疾地点了点头。

“这期间，凡格雷托雷克斯太太肯赐告的情况，我都记个大概。不过我想，她真正知道的也不会太多。”

随后他语调郑重地问她：“您本人与莱克斯顿先生夫妇相识吗？”

他是在那人正要跨出房门时提出这个问题的，格雷托雷克斯太太看见芬奇收住了脚步。很显然，他想听到她的回答。

===Original===

“I’ve never seen Mr. Lexton; but Mrs. Lexton spent a weekend at Anchorford last winter.”

Both men noticed the somewhat embarrassed way in which Roger Gretorex’s mother answered that question.

At last, reluctantly, Finch shut the door. How useful it would be, sometimes, to find oneself in two places at once!

Being the manner of woman she was, Mrs. Gretorex did not try to conceal anything of what was in her heart from her old and trusted friend.

“I am absolutely certain, Mr. Oram, that Roger had nothing to do with Mr. Lexton’s death. On the other hand, it would be dishonest to conceal from you my conviction that he is in terrible danger.”

“What makes you think that, if you are certain he is innocent?”

===Chinese===

“莱克斯顿先生我从未见过；但莱克斯顿太太去年冬天在安克福德住过一个周末。”

两个男人都注意到，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲回答这个问题时，那副颇为局促不安的模样。

芬奇终于万般无奈地带上了门。人若有时能同时身在两处，该有多有用啊！

格雷托雷克斯太太是那种性子的女人，在这位她信赖的老朋友面前，她无意隐瞒心里的一丝一毫。

“我绝对确信，奥拉姆先生，罗杰与莱克斯顿先生之死毫无干系。可另一方面，若向您隐瞒我的另一个笃信，便是不诚实的了：他正处在可怕的危险之中。”

“既然您确信他清白无辜，又凭什么这样想呢？”

===Original===

“Because,” answered Mrs. Gretorex in a low tone, “he loves this woman, Ivy Lexton, desperately. He admitted as much to me last night, before we supposed there was any fear of an immediate arrest, but after he had already had an interview with someone from Scotland Yard﻿—”

“Roger in love with a married woman. That’s the last thing I should have expected to hear!”

Mr. Oram got up. “I have a bit of business I must attend to this morning, Mrs. Gretorex. But I suggest that you wait here till a telephone message comes through from Finch.”

As they shook hands, “I beg you, I implore you,” she said in a stifled voice, “to try and believe Roger innocent.”

Mr. Oram said to himself, “I will﻿—until he is proved guilty.” Aloud he exclaimed:

“Of course I believe him innocent! But, Mrs. Gretorex, I have something very serious to say to you; that is, I feel that this is not the kind of case of which I have the necessary experience, and I doubt if I should be able to afford your son the kind of legal assistance which he needs.”

===Chinese===

“因为，”格雷托雷克斯太太低声答道，“他不顾一切地爱着这个女人，艾薇·莱克斯顿。昨夜他向我亲口承认了这一点，那时我们还想不到有立即被捕之虞，而在此之前，他已经同苏格兰场的什么人有过一回会面——”

“罗杰爱上了一个有夫之妇。这是我最不可能料到的事！”

奥拉姆先生站起身来。“今天上午我还有一桩事务必须料理，格雷托雷克斯太太。不过我建议您在这儿等一等，等芬奇那边的电话打过来。”

握手时，她声音哽咽：“我求您，我恳求您，请您试着相信罗杰是清白的。”

奥拉姆先生心里暗道：“我会的——直到他被证明有罪为止。”口中却朗声道：

“我当然相信他清白！不过，格雷托雷克斯太太，我有一句很郑重的话要对您说：我觉得，这不是我有足够经验应付的那类案子，我怀疑自己能否为令郎提供他所需要的那种法律援助。”

===Original===

He saw a look of terror and of fear flash over her face.

“Don’t desert me in my extremity!” she exclaimed. “You know as well as I do that I haven’t a single man relation in the world. You, Mr. Oram, are my only hope.” And he saw that tears were rolling down her cheeks.

“If you feel that, Mrs. Gretorex, then be assured that I shall do my best for Roger.”

===Chinese===

他看见一种惊骇惶惧的神色，从她脸上一闪而过。

“莫要在这危难关头弃我不顾啊！”她喊道，“您同我一样清楚，这世上我连一个男性亲眷也没有。您，奥拉姆先生，是我唯一的指望。”他看见泪水正滚下她的双颊。

“您既然这样想，格雷托雷克斯太太，那么请放心，我必为罗杰竭尽全力。”

## XIV / 十四

===Original===

While that, to both of them, woeful conversation was going on between the mother of Roger Gretorex and the old lawyer, Ivy Lexton sat in her drawing-room, waiting impatiently for John Oram, and﻿—his cheque.

She felt quite differently from what she had felt the day before, and happier from every point of view. For fear, that most haunting of secret housemates, had gone from her. Indeed, after seeing Mr. Oram, she had spent the rest of the afternoon at the establishment of the dressmaker who was just then the fashion in her set. Whilst there she had bought four black frocks “off the peg,” and she had also ordered a splendid fur coat.

No wonder that she was now waiting feverishly for the old lawyer to call and take her across to the bank. Two thousand pounds? What an enormous lot of money! It was the first time Ivy had had even a quarter of such a sum absolutely at her disposal. In the old days, when Jervis was still a man of means, she had never had a regular allowance. She had simply run up bills, and Jervis, grumbling good-naturedly, had paid them.

But the moments, the minutes, the quarters of an hour slipped by, and Mr. Oram dallied. What could have happened? She had become uncomfortably aware yesterday that Miles Rushworth’s solicitor did not like her, and that he thought Rushworth’s interest in her strange and inexplicable, so she began to feel thoroughly “rattled,” as she expressed it to herself.

===Chinese===

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲同老律师之间，那场令两人都黯然神伤的谈话正在进行的时候，艾薇·莱克斯顿正坐在自家客厅里，焦灼地等候着约翰·奥拉姆——还有他的支票。

她的心情同前一天已判若两人，从哪方面看都更快活了。因为恐惧——那最缠磨人的秘密同住者——已经从她身边走开了。真的，见过奥拉姆先生之后，那个下午余下的时光，她都消磨在一家时装铺子里——那正是她那个圈子眼下最时兴的一家。在那儿她买了四条现货成衣的黑裙，还订了一件华贵的裘皮大衣。

难怪她此刻坐立不安地等着老律师登门，带她去银行。两千镑？好大一笔钱哪！这么一笔款子，哪怕四分之一，能全然由她随意支配，艾薇也是生平头一遭。早年间杰维斯还是个有钱人的时候，她也从没有过一份固定的用度。她只管赊账，杰维斯便一面好脾气地嘟囔，一面把钱付清。

可是时刻一分一分、一刻一刻地溜了过去，奥拉姆先生却迟迟不来。会出了什么事呢？昨天她已不安地觉察到，迈尔斯·拉什沃思的这位律师不喜欢她，而且觉得拉什沃思对她的那份兴趣莫名其妙、无从解释，于是她开始觉得自己——用她心里的话说——彻底「慌了神」。

===Original===

At last she heard the lift stop outside the flat. What did that portend? The longed-for coming of Mr. Oram with his bountiful cheque, or more trouble for her, for poor little Ivy?

Then she gave a gasp﻿—but it was a gasp of joy, for she had heard the lawyer’s frigid voice inquiring whether she were in. Before the maid could open the door of the drawing-room she had opened it herself and exclaimed, “Is it Mr. Oram?”

She was too full of instinctive tact when dealing with any man to utter even a light word of reproach, though the solicitor was over an hour later than he had said he would be.

Mr. Oram walked into the drawing-room, and then, very deliberately, he shut the door behind him.

===Chinese===

终于，她听见电梯在公寓门外停住。这是兆着什么？是奥拉姆先生携着那张丰厚的支票，如她所盼地来了，还是她——可怜的小艾薇——又要添新的麻烦？

跟着她倒抽了一口气——却是一声欢喜，因为她听见了律师那冷冰冰的嗓音，正问她在不在家。不等女仆开门，她已自己拉开了客厅的门，扬声喊道：「是奥拉姆先生吗？」

同男人打交道时她天生满腹机变，这位律师虽说比约定时间晚了一个多钟头，她连一句轻轻的责备也不出口。

奥拉姆先生走进客厅，随即，不慌不忙地，反手关上了门。

===Original===

Again there came over Ivy a sick feeling of fear. He looked stern, forbidding, and as a certain kind of man looks when he is the bearer of bad news.

“I’m sorry I’m late,” he said abruptly, “but I couldn’t help myself. I’ve brought the cheque, and we will proceed in a few moments to the bank. But first I would like to tell you, Mrs. Lexton, that circumstances have arisen that will make it impossible for me to act as your lawyer with regard to any proceedings that may arise in connection with your husband’s death.”

He cleared his throat, and then went on: “As I cannot act for you, I will find you a first-class man, who will probably have far more time to devote to your affairs than I should have been able to do.”

She looked at him, wondering what this really meant, and a tide of dismay welled up in her heart.

===Chinese===

一阵发呕的恐惧再次袭上艾薇心头。他神情严峻、冷面拒人，正像某一种男人捎来坏消息时的那副模样。

「抱歉我来迟了，」他唐突地说，「可我实在没法子。支票我带来了，过几分钟咱们就去银行。不过在此之前，我想告诉您，莱克斯顿太太：事态有了变化，凡是牵涉您丈夫之死而可能引起的诉讼，我都不能再担任您的律师了。」

他清了清嗓子，接着说：「我既不能为您办事，就会替您寻一位一流的人物，他花在您的事务上的工夫，多半会远比我所能抽出的多。」

她望着他，猜不透这话究竟是什么意思，一股惶乱的潮水从心底涌了上来。

===Original===

“But Mr. Rushworth,” she began falteringly, “again told me, in a cable that I received only this morning, that you would do everything you could for me, Mr. Oram?”

She had not meant to tell anyone of that long, intimately-worded cable, the first in which Rushworth had allowed something of his intense exultation at the knowledge that she was now free to pierce through the measured words. It seemed to her impossible that anyone could disregard the wishes of so important and, above all, so wealthy a man as Miles Rushworth. To Ivy the sound of money talking drowned every other sound in life. But this, to her discomfiture, was not the case with John Oram.

“I know that,” and this time he spoke more kindly. “And I’m sorry I shall not be able to do what Mr. Rushworth very naturally hoped I could do. But I have discovered﻿—” and then he stopped for what seemed to her a long time.

He was wondering whether she was yet aware that Roger Gretorex had been arrested on the charge of having murdered her husband. Already the fact was billed in all the early editions of the evening papers.

===Chinese===

「可是拉什沃思先生，」她结结巴巴地开口道，「就在今早我才收到的电报里，还重新对我说过，您会尽力帮我一切的，奥拉姆先生？」

那封措辞亲昵的长电报，她本没打算说给任何人听。那是头一回，拉什沃思容许了那么一点他那抑制不住的狂喜——因得知她如今自由了而生的狂喜——穿透那字斟句酌的电文透了出来。在她看来，谁也不能无视像迈尔斯·拉什沃思这样一位大人物、尤其是这样一位有钱人的意愿。在艾薇听来，金钱开口说话的声音，盖得过生活里其余的一切声响。可是叫她狼狈的是，约翰·奥拉姆偏偏不吃这一套。

「这我知道，」这一回他语气和气了些，「拉什沃思先生自然盼着我能效劳，我办不到，也很抱歉。可是我发现——」说到这里他停住了，在她看来一停便停了许久。

他正在盘算，她是否已经知道罗杰·格雷托雷克斯已被逮捕，罪名是谋害她的丈夫。这件事早已刊在今晚各报的所有早期版面上，满城招贴了。

===Original===

“The truth is,” he began again, and in a colder tone, “not only I, but my father before me, and my grandfather before him, acted in a legal capacity for the Gretorex family.”

The colour rushed into Ivy’s face. She said defensively, “But need that make any difference, Mr. Oram?”

“Well, yes, I’m sorry to say that it will, Mrs. Lexton. Roger Gretorex, as you are no doubt aware, was arrested last night on a charge of having poisoned Mr. Jervis Lexton. He has put his interests in my hands. It would not be to your advantage were you to employ the same solicitor as the man who is accused of having murdered your husband. I am sure,” he cleared his throat, “you are aware of what Dr. Gretorex’s motive is supposed to have been, assuming that he is guilty of that of which he is accused?”

Ivy looked so frightened that for a moment he thought she was going to faint.

===Chinese===

「实情是，」他又开口了，声调更冷，「不单是我，先父在我之前，祖父又在先父之前，都是格雷托雷克斯家的法律代理人。」

血色涌上艾薇的脸。她分辩道：「可这有什么妨碍呢，奥拉姆先生？」

「嗯，有的，很遗憾，确有妨碍，莱克斯顿太太。罗杰·格雷托雷克斯昨晚被捕，罪名是毒死杰维斯·莱克斯顿先生，这您想必已经知道。他已把自己的利益托付到我手里。您若雇用与这个被控谋杀您丈夫的人同一位律师，于您并无好处。我确信，」他清了清嗓子，「人们推测格雷托雷克斯医生的作案动机是什么，您是知道的——倘若他被控的罪名成立的话？」

艾薇显得那样惊惶，一时他竟以为她要晕过去了。

===Original===

Then she hadn’t known of Gretorex’s arrest? Even John Oram, who was already strongly prejudiced against her, could not doubt that the horror and distress with which she heard his news were genuine.

She sank down into a chair.

“But this is terrible﻿—terrible!” she moaned.

“It is terrible, Mrs. Lexton. And, incidentally, you see, now, how I am situated? When I came here to see you yesterday, I naturally did not associate my friend and client, Dr. Roger Gretorex, with the strange and mysterious circumstances surrounding your husband’s death. I have not yet seen a copy of the statement you appear to have made to the inspector who came to see you from Scotland Yard; but I gather that you made certain admissions that were very detrimental to my client.”

===Chinese===

这么说，她原先并不知道格雷托雷克斯被捕的事？就连对她早怀深重偏见的约翰·奥拉姆，也不能怀疑，她听着这个消息时的惊骇与悲痛不是出于真心。

她颓然跌坐进一把椅子里。

「这太可怕了——太可怕了！」她呜咽道。

「是可怕，莱克斯顿太太。您也顺便看清了我的处境了吧？昨天我来见您的时候，自然没有把我的朋友兼当事人罗杰·格雷托雷克斯医生，同您丈夫之死周围那些离奇神秘的情形联系起来。您似乎向苏格兰场来见您的那个探长作了陈述，我还没有见到副本；但我得知，您招认了几件事，对我的当事人十分不利。」

===Original===

“The man pressed me so! I didn’t want to hurt Roger,” she exclaimed, and thought she spoke the truth.

Twenty minutes later, as the two came out of the bank, Mr. Oram said quietly:

“With your permission, Mrs. Lexton, I am going to put you in touch with an old friend of mine, a most able lawyer named Paxton-Smith. He will not only watch your interests in a general sense, but you can trust him to give you the soundest advice. In your place, I would make a point of being frank with him concerning everything connected with your husband’s life as well as with his death.”

It was strange what a feeling of repugnance, almost of horror, this beautiful girl﻿—for she looked a girl﻿—inspired in him. But that, so he told himself, for he tried to be fair-minded, was no doubt owing to the way Roger Gretorex’s mother had spoken of Ivy Lexton that morning.

===Chinese===

「那个人逼得我好紧！我并不想伤害罗杰啊，」她喊道，并且自以为说的全是实话。

二十分钟后，两人从银行出来，奥拉姆先生平静地说：

「如果您允许，莱克斯顿太太，我想把您托给我的一位老友——一位极干练的律师，名叫帕克斯顿-史密斯（Paxton-Smith）。他不但会一般性地照应您的利益，而且您尽可信赖他给您出最稳妥的主意。我若在您的位置，就一定对他坦诚相告：凡与您丈夫生前及死时相关的一切，无一隐瞒。」

说来奇怪，这个美丽的姑娘——她看上去就像个姑娘——竟叫他生出一种近乎憎惧的厌恶之感。可他对自己说——他也力求存心公道——这多半是缘于那天早晨罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲说起艾薇·莱克斯顿时的那番口吻。

===Original===

“Tell Mr. Paxton-Smith, as far you know it, the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth,” he went on. “Many ladies, when in conference with their legal adviser, are tempted to hold something back. There can be no greater mistake. You can be absolutely sure of Mr. Paxton-Smith’s discretion; and unless he knows everything you can tell him, it will be impossible for him to advise you adequately.”

She was gazing at him with affrighted eyes. Tell the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth? Why, she couldn’t even begin to think of doing that! But, even so, the old lawyer’s words impressed her. Why, oh! why, had she been tempted to tell the man from Scotland Yard that half-truth as to Roger and his love for her? By now, when it had become clear to her that no one suspected her, she had almost forgotten what had brought about that dangerous admission.

“You are, I understand, going to be the chief witness for the Crown,” said Mr. Oram solemnly.

“I didn’t know that! What does being that mean?” faltered Ivy.

===Chinese===

「把您所知的真相告诉帕克斯顿-史密斯先生——真相，全部真相，除真相外别无他言，」他接着说，「许多女士同法律顾问面谈时，总忍不住要瞒下点什么。再没有比这更错的了。帕克斯顿-史密斯先生的谨严您尽可百分百放心；他若不知道您能告诉他的一切，便无法给您出周全的主意。」

她睁着一双惊恐的眼睛直望着他。真相，全部真相，除真相外别无他言？哎呀，她连想一想都无从想起！可即便如此，老律师的话还是打动了她。唉，为什么，噢，为什么，她当时竟忍不住要对那个苏格兰场来的人，说出关于罗杰和他对她的爱情的那半真半假的话？到如今，她已明白谁也不曾疑心她，当初是着了什么魔才招出那句危险的话来，她几乎已经忘了。

「我听说，您要做控方的头号证人了，」奥拉姆先生郑重地说。

「我不知道这个！做那个是什么意思？」艾薇颤声问。

===Original===

“It means,” he said drily, “that the prosecution is counting on you to aid them in proving that Roger Gretorex became that most despicable of human beings, a slow, secret poisoner, in order that you might be free to become his wife.”

She unconsciously stayed her steps, and was staring up at him as if hypnotised by his words.

He looked down fixedly into her face. What lay hidden behind those lovely eyes, that exquisite little mouth, now spoilt, according to his taste, by a smear of scarlet paint?

“Only God knows the secrets of all hearts, Mrs. Lexton. I have not asked you, and I do not propose to ask you, if you believe that unhappy young man to be guilty of the fearful crime of which he is accused, and for which he is about to stand on trial for his life. But if there is any doubt in your mind, and, far more, if you believe him innocent, I beg you, earnestly, to consider and weigh every word you utter from now on.”

===Chinese===

「这意味着，」他干巴巴地说，「控方正指望您助他们一臂之力，证明罗杰·格雷托雷克斯成了人类中最可鄙的那一种东西——一个下手缓慢、暗中进行投毒的毒物犯——好叫您得以脱身自由，去做他的妻子。」

她不由自主地收住脚步，仰起眼直盯着他，仿佛被他的一席话摄住了魂。

他低头定定地凝视她的脸。那双可爱的眼睛、那只精巧的小嘴后面，究竟藏着什么呢？——只是照他的眼光看来，那张小嘴如今已被一抹猩红的唇膏糟蹋了。

「唯有上帝洞悉万人心中的隐秘，莱克斯顿太太。我未曾问过您，也不打算问您，是否相信那个不幸的年轻人犯下了他被控的那桩可怕罪行——他即将为此案出庭，生死在此一搏。但只要您心中有半点疑惑，尤其若您相信他是无辜的，我便恳切地求您，从今往后，斟酌掂量您说出的每一个字。」

===Original===

But, even as he made that appeal, moved out of his usual cautious self by his real regard for Roger Gretorex and his intense pity for Roger’s mother, he felt convinced that Ivy Lexton would, in all circumstances and contingencies, only consider herself and what was to her own advantage.

How amazing that such a man as was Miles Rushworth should be moved to passion by such a frivolous, mindless, selfish woman! But that such was the case John Oram had far too much knowledge of human nature to doubt, even for a moment. He was, indeed, by now as sure as was Ivy herself that, in due course, Mrs. Jervis Lexton would become Mrs. Miles Rushworth.

Suddenly Ivy said something which very much surprised her companion, and made him dislike her even more than he already disliked her.

“Are you going to cable everything that has happened to Mr. Rushworth?” she asked in a frightened tone.

===Chinese===

然而，就在他发出这番恳求的时候——他对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的真诚敬重、对罗杰母亲的深切怜悯，使他一时脱出了平日那副谨慎的自家——他心底却确信，无论情势如何变幻，艾薇·莱克斯顿只会想到她自己，只会想到什么于她自己有利。

像迈尔斯·拉什沃思这样一个人，竟会被这样一个轻浮、没脑子、自私的女人勾起如火情热，多么叫人惊叹！但这确是事实——约翰·奥拉姆对人情世故懂得太多，连片刻也不曾怀疑这一点。的确，此刻他已同艾薇本人一样确信：杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太到头来终会成为迈尔斯·拉什沃思太太。

艾薇忽然说出一句叫同伴大出意外的话来，使他比先前已经不喜欢她的程度，愈发不喜欢她了。

「发生的一切，您都要打电报告诉拉什沃思先生吗？」她问，声调里带着惊惶。

===Original===

“Mr. Rushworth will learn precisely what the Cape Town newspapers choose to publish, and what you choose to cable to him. He has not asked me to communicate with him, and I am not proposing to do so.”

He held out his hand. “And now I must say goodbye, Mrs. Lexton. I will try to arrange that Mr. Paxton-Smith shall ring you up before lunch. He will then make an appointment to see you. I should like, if I may, to give you one word of advice. It is this. Refuse, however great the temptation, to disclose anything that concerns your husband’s death to anyone, excepting, of course, to Mr. Paxton-Smith.”

“Then shan’t I see you again?” she asked.

Though deep in her heart she was glad to be seeing the last of Mr. Oram, she knew him to be her only link, in London, with Miles Rushworth.

“Should Mr. Rushworth cable me instructions to do so, I shall of course transmit to you any money or any messages he may choose to send through me. But, apart from that, it is clear that in your own interest Roger Gretorex’s legal adviser should have no more communication with you.”

===Chinese===

「拉什沃思先生会原原本本得知，开普敦（Cape Town）各报愿意刊登些什么，以及您愿意打给他些什么。他并没有要我同他通消息，我也不打算那样做。」

他伸出手来。「现在我得告辞了，莱克斯顿太太。我设法让帕克斯顿-史密斯先生午饭前给您去电话，那时他再同您约时见面。如果允许，我想赠您一句忠告。就是：无论诱惑多大，一概拒绝向任何人吐露任何牵涉您丈夫之死的事——当然，帕克斯顿-史密斯先生除外。」

「那我再也见不着您了？」她问。

虽然她心底深处巴不得就此送走奥拉姆先生、再不见他，可她也知道，在伦敦，他是她同迈尔斯·拉什沃思之间唯一的纽带。

「倘若拉什沃思先生来电报吩咐，那么凡他愿意经我转交的钱款或口信，我自然一一转到您手上。但除此之外，很显然，为您自身的利益计，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的法律顾问不该再与您有任何往来。」

---

===Original===

That same afternoon Philip Paxton-Smith had his first interview with Ivy Lexton. Unlike John Oram, he took an instant fancy to the prettiest client and most attractive little woman, so he told himself, that a Providence which was apt to be kind in that way to the shrewd and popular solicitor had ever sent his way.

So it was that, after a very few moments, Ivy found herself chatting to him almost happily.

He listened with unaffected, indeed absorbed, interest to her sentimental half-true, half-false, account of her first meeting with Roger Gretorex. Of how the young man had “fallen for her” at once, and how she had seen coming, and tried to stave off, his declaration of passionate love.

She also managed to convey to her new friend’s sympathetic ears what manner of man she now desired Jervis Lexton to be supposed to have been. Easygoing, good-tempered, devoted to her, and yet entirely selfish, frightfully extravagant, and, when they were not out together enjoying a good time, a great deal at his club.

===Chinese===

就在当天下午，菲利普·帕克斯顿-史密斯（Philip Paxton-Smith）同艾薇·莱克斯顿作了第一次面谈。同约翰·奥拉姆不同，他一见便喜欢上了这位当事人——照他心里的说法，老天爷一向惯用这类恩典照顾他这位精明而得人缘的律师，而这回送到他跟前的，是最漂亮的委托人、最动人的小女人。

于是不出几分钟，艾薇便发觉自己几乎快快活活地同他聊了起来。

他毫不做作、简直入了神地听她动情地讲她同罗杰·格雷托雷克斯初识的经过——半真半假。讲那年轻人如何一眼便「迷上了她」，她又如何眼看着那番炽烈的爱情表白逼来，如何躲闪招架。

她还设法让这位新朋友那双富有同情的耳朵，听进了她此刻但愿世人相信杰维斯·莱克斯顿生前是怎样一种人：性情随和，脾气好，疼她爱她，却又彻头彻尾自私，挥霍得吓人；两人不一道出门寻欢作乐的时候，他便大把时光泡在俱乐部里。

===Original===

“Poor lonely little woman,” said the lawyer to himself. “The real wonder is that she remained as straight as she did.”

Paxton-Smith and his partner did a very different class of business from that associated with the firm of which John Oram was now senior partner. They were constantly associated with what are loosely called “society cases,” and Paxton-Smith himself, something of a gay bachelor, was seen a good deal in that section of the London world which seems to live for pleasure. He was well liked by men. As for women, well, he liked women﻿—and they liked him too.

During his first interview with Ivy Lexton, after he had, as he believed, won her entire confidence, he cleverly led her to give an almost verbatim report of the conversation which she had had with Inspector Orpington. And though once or twice he shook his head when he heard what she had admitted, he was able to do to her what he failed to do to himself, that is, make her believe that, on the whole, she had been wise rather than unwise in her dealings with the man in whose charge had been the preliminary inquiries concerning her husband’s death.

Philip Paxton-Smith was both a clever man and a clever lawyer. But “this dear little woman,” as he already called her to himself, was more than a match for him. How amazed would he have been could some entity outside himself have been able to convince him, at the end of the two and a half hours that he spent with Ivy Lexton that afternoon, that she had, as a matter of fact, so completely deceived him as to make him believe her everything she was not!

===Chinese===

「可怜的孤单单的小女人，」律师心里说，「真正的奇迹倒在于，她居然还守身如玉到这个地步。」

帕克斯顿-史密斯同他的合伙人做的生意，与约翰·奥拉姆如今身为高级合伙人的那家事务所大不相同。他们经手的多是些被宽泛地称作「社交界案件」的官司，而帕克斯顿-史密斯本人算得上个快活的单身汉，伦敦那个仿佛专为寻欢作乐而活的圈子里，常常见得着他的身影。男人们都喜欢他。至于女人么，唔，他喜欢女人——女人们也喜欢他。

同艾薇·莱克斯顿的第一次面谈中，他自信已赢得了她的全部信任，便巧妙地引着她说出她同奥平顿探长那番谈话几乎一字不差的逐句汇报。虽然听到她招认的内容时，他有一两次摇了摇头，但他对她做成了一件事——一件他对自己偏偏做不到的事：使她相信，同那个主持她丈夫之死初步调查的人周旋，总的来说她做得明智，而非失策。

菲利普·帕克斯顿-史密斯既是个聪明人，又是个精明的律师。可是这位他已经私底下唤作「亲爱的小女人」的当事人，却比他更胜一筹。那天下午他同艾薇·莱克斯顿一起消磨了两个半钟头，倘若有什么身外之物能在这时候让他明白：事实上她已把他骗得那么彻底，骗得他相信她具备她恰恰没有的一切品性——他该会何等惊愕啊！

===Original===

True, he had begun by thinking her just a little stupid; but he had ended by realising that she was far more intelligent than many of the women with whom he was in contact. That, naturally, had made him like her all the more, for there is nothing more tiresome or annoying to any good lawyer than having to deal with a dull and obstinate client.

As for Ivy, she was happier after Paxton-Smith had left her than she had felt since the terrible moment when the card of Inspector Orpington had first been brought in to her.

Not only did the genial lawyer inspire her with confidence, but she was naturally pleased and relieved to feel that he believed everything she told him. It was such a comfort, such a moral support, to feel that he “liked” her, and that he was going to do his very best to help her through what even she now realised was going to be a dangerous and anxious time.

===Chinese===

不错，他起初只当她有点儿蠢；可到头来他明白了，她比他往来的许多女人都聪明得多。这自然使他愈发喜欢她，因为对任何好律师来说，再没有比伺候一个又蠢又固执的当事人更叫人厌烦气闷的了。

至于艾薇，帕克斯顿-史密斯走后，她比奥平顿探长的名片初次递到她面前那可怕一刻以来的任何时候，都更快活。

这位和蔼可亲的律师不仅令她生出信任，而且她说的什么他信什么，自然又叫她高兴又叫她宽心。感到他「喜欢」她、感到他必会竭尽全力帮她捱过这一段——连她自己也明白将要临头的——凶险忧煎的时日，是何等的安慰，何等的精神支柱啊。

---

===Original===

By the morning following the day of Ivy’s first memorable interview with her lawyer, it was obvious to all those concerned with the case that what was already called “The Lexton Mystery” was going to develop into a cause célèbre.

Already the personality of Jervis Lexton’s young widow was becoming of moment, indeed of absorbing interest, to hundreds of thousands of newspaper readers. And as the dark, early winter days slipped by, men and women engaged in wordy combat as to whether she was the sweet, wholly innocent, guileless woman portrayed by her admirers, or a typical example of the selfish, heartless, extravagant little minx old-fashioned folk are wont to describe the modern girl and young woman.

Very soon her exquisite face became as familiar to the public as those of the more popular actresses. She, not Roger Gretorex, emerged as the central figure of this drama of love and death; this if only because she was, to the mind of everyone interested in the story, the one point of mystery.

Had she loved in secret the good-looking young doctor who had almost certainly slowly, craftily, done to death the man who was now described as having been his best friend? Or was it, as she was said to aver, the truth that Gretorex had adored her with no touch of encouragement on her part?

===Chinese===

艾薇同她的律师作了那第一次难忘的面谈，到第二天早晨，凡与本案有牵连的人都已看得明白：这件已经被人称作「莱克斯顿疑案」的案子，注定要闹成一桩轰动一时的名案（cause célèbre）。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿的年轻遗孀其人，已经在成千上万的报纸读者中间变得举足轻重起来——说实在的，成了人人瞩目的焦点。随着初冬那些昏暗的日子一天天过去，男男女女们唇枪舌剑地争个不休：她究竟是仰慕者们描绘的那种甜美、全然无辜、毫无城府的女人呢，还是那种典型的自私、无心肝、挥霍无度的小狐狸精——这等字眼，正是老派人物惯于拿来形容摩登女郎和新时代的年轻女性的。

不多时，她那张精巧的脸便同走红的女伶们一样为公众所熟悉。成了这出爱与死的戏剧的中心人物的，不是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，而是她；哪怕只因为这一点：在关心这段故事的每个人心里，她才是那唯一的谜。

她可曾偷偷爱恋过那个英俊的年轻医生——如今几乎可以断定，正是他慢慢地、狡诈地将那个现在被说成是他最好朋友的男人送了终？还是如她所断言的才是实情：格雷托雷克斯单恋痴缠，而她这边没有半分怂恿招引？

===Original===

Here and there some were found ready to whisper that perhaps “Ivy” had been “in it.” But they were a small minority of the vast public interested in such a story, and, for the most part, they kept their view to themselves. It is a dangerous thing to libel the living﻿—especially a woman blessed with as many ardent champions as was Jervis Lexton’s widow.

And Ivy herself? For a little while Ivy remained unaware of the amazing interest which was already being taken in her past thoughts, past secret emotions, and past way of life.

She went through many anxious, troubled solitary hours till, all at once, those acquaintances, both men and women, who become friends at a moment’s notice, crowded round her, pleased and excited to be associated with so terrible and mysterious an affair.

Their new mission in life, so they pretended to themselves and others, was to try to cheer up poor pretty little Ivy in her solitude. It must be admitted that these, for the most part, young friends, succeeded in their laudable object to an extent that sometimes amazed Paxton-Smith.

===Chinese===

东一处西一处，也有些人凑在耳朵边嘀咕，说「艾薇」说不定「也有份儿」。但在关注这类故事的浩浩公众里，他们只占一小撮，而且多半把自家的看法闷在肚里。诽谤活人是桩危险的事——尤其是诽谤一个像杰维斯·莱克斯顿的遗孀这样拥有众多热烈卫护者的女人。

那么艾薇自己呢？有一小段日子，艾薇还不知道，世人已经对她过去的念头、过去的隐秘情感、过去的生活方式，生出了莫大的兴致。

她捱过了许多忧惧烦乱的孤寂时辰，直到忽然之间，那些一招呼便能立刻升作朋友的男女相识把她团团围住，个个欢天喜地、兴奋不已，为能同这样一桩骇人听闻的神秘案子沾上边而得意。

他们此番人生的新使命——他们对自己也对旁人如此标榜——就是要在孤寂中给可怜的漂亮小艾薇解闷逗乐。必得承认，这些多半年轻的朋友们，把他们那桩可嘉的事业办得颇有成效，有时连帕克斯顿-史密斯都为之咋舌。

===Original===

Those giddy, good-natured, carelessly heartless men and women all took so absolutely for granted the fact that Roger Gretorex had committed murder for love of Ivy Lexton. Some of the men about her indeed hinted with a half-smile that they could well understand the poor chap’s motive.

One of them, the well-to-do idle bachelor who had been about with her so much this last autumn, and who was acting as her cavalier at the theatre and supper party on the evening of Jervis’s death, actually said to her, “I wouldn’t back myself not to have done the same thing in his place! After all, ‘opportunity makes the crime.’ ”

But, if comforted, she had also been stung by that well-worn saying; for she knew, better than anyone in the whole world just now, how great is the awful truth embodied in those trite words.

The women who gathered round Ivy Lexton during those days were not all inspired by morbid curiosity and excitement. Especially was Lady Flora Desmond very kind to her.

===Chinese===

这些轻浮、好心、无心无肺的男男女女，全都把一件事视作理所当然：罗杰·格雷托雷克斯是为了爱艾薇·莱克斯顿而行的凶。她身边有些男人甚至还半含笑意地暗示，那可怜家伙的动机，他们太能理解了。

其中一个——就是去年秋天长伴她出游的那位阔绰游闲的单身汉，杰维斯死的那晚在戏院和夜宴上给她充任护花使者的那一位——竟对她说：「换了我是他，我也不敢担保自己不干出同样的事来！说到底，『机会造罪行』嘛。」

可是这句话虽让她得了安慰，也让她挨了蜇；因为这句老生常谈里裹着的可怕真理有多大分量，此刻普天之下，再没有第二个人比她知道得更清楚了。

那些日子里聚拢在艾薇·莱克斯顿身边的女人，倒也并非个个都被病态的好奇与兴奋驱使。弗洛拉·德斯蒙德小姐待她就尤其好。

===Original===

Lady Flora thought she understood something of what Mrs. Lexton must be feeling because she herself had gone through such a terrible agony when her husband had died. Lady Flora was a real support to Ivy during those long days of waiting for the day when she would be compelled to appear as chief witness for the Crown at the trial of Roger Gretorex.

True, this kind friend wanted to take her away to her country cottage, but Ivy refused with quiet obstinacy. She knew that she would go melancholy mad were she left with no one to talk to except one affectionate, sympathising woman friend.

Mrs. Jervis Lexton had plenty of good excuses for remaining in London, for, during the comparatively short time which elapsed between Gretorex’s arrest and the opening of his trial, there took place the long-drawn-out legal formalities of which the public are only aware to an extent which whets, without satisfying, curiosity.

But at last everything was “in order”; so Paxton-Smith put it to the woman to whom he found himself devoting far more thought, as well as time, than he had ever done before to any client, however charming, in his successful legal career.

===Chinese===

弗洛拉小姐自以为颇能体会莱克斯顿太太此刻的心情，因为她自己丈夫死时，也曾捱过那样一场可怕的煎熬。在那段翘首等着出庭之日的漫长日子里——她到时将不得不以控方头号证人的身份，出现在罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的审判庭上——弗洛拉小姐是艾薇实实在在的依靠。

这位好心的朋友固然想带她去自己乡间的小屋住，艾薇却安安静静地执意不肯。她知道，若是身边只剩一位情意绵绵、一味怜惜的闺中女友可谈，她非闷出一场忧郁的疯病来不可。

杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太留着伦敦不走的现成借口有的是：从格雷托雷克斯被捕到开审之间那段不算太长的日子里，种种拖拖拉拉的法律程序接踵而来，而公众对它们的所知，恰恰只到勾起好奇而永不能满足的地步。

但终于一切都「就绪」了——帕克斯顿-史密斯如此对那位女子说。他发觉，自己在这位当事人身上费的心思和光阴，比他功成名就的律师生涯里任何一位当事人——无论多么迷人——都要多得多。

===Original===

The shrewd solicitor sometimes felt something like angry contempt for the foolish, selfish, talkative men and women who buzzed round the young widow during these, to him, anxious and tiring days. He supposed himself, naturally enough, to be just now her only real standby in life.

How amazed, how piqued, he would have been, had some tricksy spirit whispered in his ear the news that every morning, and sometimes in the evening too, Ivy received a long cable of sympathy, support, and even, as the days went on, of disguised passion!

Of that passion of love which can assume so infinite a variety of shapes and disguises, Ivy Lexton had had many an exciting experience, but none so satisfying as that conveyed thousands of miles, and in a shadowy form, from the man for whom, as she now dimly realised, she had run at any rate the risk of a shameful and horrible death.

===Chinese===

在这些于他焦心劳神的日子里，这位精明的律师有时对围着年轻遗孀嗡嗡营营的那伙愚蠢、自私、多嘴多舌的男男女女，几乎生出愤愤的轻蔑来。他自然而然地认定，眼下她生活里唯一真正的靠山，唯有他一个。

倘若有什么促狭鬼附在他耳边报信，说每天早晨——有时晚上也有——艾薇都会收到一封长长的电报，满怀同情与支持，而且随着日子一天天过去，还裹着掩饰不住的如火情热！他该多么惊愕，多么恼火啊！

爱情的这种热狂能变幻出无穷无尽的形貌与伪装，艾薇·莱克斯顿阅历过的刺激体验不可谓不多，却从没有一样像这样称心遂意——从千万里外、隔着一片朦胧传到她身边，来自那个男人；而为了这个男人，正如她如今隐约明白的，她至少是甘冒过一场身败名裂、死得凄惨的风险的。

---

===Original===

Ivy, for once, was quite alone, lying down in the drawing-room, reading a magazine, one evening, when suddenly the door opened.

“Mrs. Gretorex wishes to see you, madam,” said the maid in a nervous tone.

Ivy leapt up from the sofa, and by the shaded light of the reading lamp which had stood close to her elbow, she saw a tall, spectral-looking figure advancing into the room.

But it was a firm and very clear voice that exclaimed: “I did not write and ask you to receive me, Mrs. Lexton, as I feared you might say ‘No.’ I’m not acting on Mr. Oram’s advice in thus coming to see you, but I know he doubts, as I do too, if you are really aware in what deadly danger my son now stands?”

===Chinese===

一天傍晚，艾薇难得独自一人，正躺在客厅里翻一本杂志，房门忽然开了。

「格雷托雷克斯太太想见您，太太，」女仆说，声音里带着紧张。

艾薇从沙发上跳起来。就着立在臂弯旁那盏阅读灯的罩光，她看见一个高高的、幽灵般的人影正往屋里走来。

可开口的却是一副坚定而清朗的嗓音：「我没有写信求您接待我，莱克斯顿太太，因为怕您会回一个『不』字。我这样登门，并非奉奥拉姆先生之命，可我知道，他跟我一样存着疑心：您可真的明白，我儿子眼下正站在怎样的致命险境里？」

===Original===

“Do sit down,” murmured Ivy.

She felt a surge of angry fear of Roger Gretorex’s mother, but she had quickly made up her mind to be what she called “sweet” to her unwelcome visitor. When, always against her will, the thought of Gretorex forced itself on her mind, there was coupled with it the terrifying perception that by now he must be well aware of who it was who had brought about the death of Jervis Lexton.

“Appearances,” said Mrs. Gretorex in a low, quiet voice, “are very much against Roger. His counsel is thinking, we understand, of putting forward a theory that your husband committed suicide. I have come to ask you if you can advance anything to add even a tinge of probability to that theory? Was there insanity in Mr. Lexton’s family? Did he, above all, say, even once, that he might be tempted to take his life?”

These clear, passionless questions gave Ivy no opportunity for the display of her special gifts. She asked herself nervously what she ought to say in answer to these definite queries.

===Chinese===

「快请坐，」艾薇低声道。

她对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲涌起一阵又气又怕的情绪，可她已飞快打定主意，要用她所谓的「甜」来应付这位不受欢迎的来客。每当格雷托雷克斯的影子违拗她的意愿硬挤进脑海，随之而来的总有一种叫她胆寒的知觉：事到如今，他必定已经清清楚楚地知道，究竟是谁送了杰维斯·莱克斯顿的性命。

「种种迹象，」格雷托雷克斯太太低声平静地说，「都对罗杰很不利。我们听说，他的出庭律师正盘算着提出一种说法，说您丈夫是自寻短见的。我来是想请问您：您可有什么能拿出来的，好给这说法添上哪怕一丝半毫的可信？莱克斯顿先生家里有没有人得过精神病？尤其是，他可曾——哪怕一回——说过，他兴许会禁不住寻了短见？」

这些清清楚楚、不动声色的问话，让艾薇没有机会施展她的特殊本领。她心里发慌地掂量着：面对这些明明白白的问题，究竟该怎样回答。

===Original===

Would it be to her interest to allow it to be thought that she, at any rate, believed it possible that Jervis had done away with himself? Then she decided that, no, it would not pay her to accept what everyone who had ever come in contact with her husband, including Nurse Bradfield, and the two doctors who had been attending him, would know to be impossible. So:

“I never heard him say anything of that sort,” she answered regretfully, “except in fun, of course.” She added, as an afterthought, “But I know how much Jervis hated to be poor, Mrs. Gretorex.”

The older woman threw an imperceptible look round the luxurious room.

“But he wasn’t poor,” she said quickly. “He had just got, or so we understand, a good new post.”

===Chinese===

让人以为她至少相信杰维斯可能自寻了短见——这样于她有利吗？她随即断定：不，划不来。凡同她丈夫打过交道的人，连同布拉德菲尔德护士和两位诊治他的医生，谁都知道那绝不可能，认下这桩事就等于自讨没趣。于是：

「我从来没有听他说过这样的话，」她答道，语气里带着憾意，「当然，玩笑话是有的。」她又补了一句，像是事后想起来的，「可我知道杰维斯多么恨穷，格雷托雷克斯太太。」

年长的女人把这间奢华的客厅不着痕迹地扫了一眼。

「可他并不穷啊，」她马上说，「他不是刚谋得一个体面的新职位么——至少我们听说如此。」

===Original===

“I know he had. That’s one of the things that makes it all so dreadful﻿—”

And then Mrs. Gretorex, who was herself a very honest woman, felt impelled to ask what was perhaps a dangerous question.

“I need hardly ask you what you think? Whatever be the truth, you do not believe, Mrs. Lexton, that my son poisoned your husband?”

Ivy did not answer for what seemed to Mrs. Gretorex a long, long time. Then she exclaimed, twisting her fingers together:

===Chinese===

「我知道他谋得了。这正是叫这一切显得如此可怕的原因之一——」

这时，格雷托雷克斯太太——她自己是个非常诚实的人——觉得不由得要问出一个也许危险的问题来。

「我心里怎么想，何必再问您呢？不管真相怎样，您总归不相信，莱克斯顿太太，是我儿子毒死了您的丈夫吧？」

艾薇半晌没有作声——在格雷托雷克斯太太看来，那是很长很长的一阵子。然后她绞着手指，喊了起来：

===Original===

“It’s no good asking me that sort of thing, because I honestly don’t know what to think. It’s all so strange!”

“But surely you know Roger to be innocent?”

Ivy let her eyes drop.

“Of course, I want to think that,” she said in a low tone.

===Chinese===

「这种事问我没用，因为我实实在在不知道该怎么想。这一切太蹊跷了！」

「可您总该知道罗杰是无辜的吧？」

艾薇垂下了眼帘。

「我当然愿意这么想，」她低声说。

===Original===

“You want to think it, Mrs. Lexton? D’you mean that you have any doubt about it?”

Again Ivy twisted her fingers together.

“It’s all so strange,” she repeated falteringly. “And it’s so unfortunate that Dr. Gretorex was the last one to see my husband alone on the day he died.”

Mrs. Gretorex got up.

===Chinese===

「您愿意这么想，莱克斯顿太太？您是说，您对此还有怀疑？」

艾薇又绞起了手指。

「这一切太蹊跷了，」她结结巴巴地重复道。「偏偏又是格雷托雷克斯医生，是死的那天最后单独见我丈夫的人。真是不幸得很。」

格雷托雷克斯太太站起身来。

===Original===

“I see,” she said in a dull tone. “Then you are half inclined to believe that Roger did do this terrible thing﻿—for love, I suppose, of you?”

And there flashed a look of awful condemnation over the mother’s worn face.

“Please don’t say that, Mrs. Gretorex! I never said that I thought poor Roger really did it!” cried Ivy hysterically. “Perhaps Jervis did commit suicide, but, as nurse says, if he did poison himself, where did he get the stuff to do it with? Also Roger was so fearfully gone on me. It’s all so very, very strange!”

Oh, why had Mrs. Gretorex come here, just to torture her and frighten her? It was too cruel!

===Chinese===

「我明白了，」她声音喑哑地说。「那么您是有些倾向于相信，罗杰真的干下了这桩可怕的事——我想，是为了对您的爱？」

母亲那张憔悴的脸上，倏地闪过一道可怕的谴责之色。

「千万别这么说，格雷托雷克斯太太！我从没说过我觉得可怜的罗杰真干了那事！」艾薇歇斯底里地喊道。「也许杰维斯真是自尽的，可是照护士的说法，他要是自己服了毒，那毒药是从哪儿弄来的？再说罗杰又是那样死心塌地地迷着我。这一切实在太、太蹊跷了！」

啊，格雷托雷克斯太太为什么要上这里来，专门来折磨她、吓唬她？这太狠心了！

===Original===

Then Roger Gretorex’s mother did make to the woman who stood before her, this woman whom her son loved to his undoing, a desperate appeal, though she worded what she had to say quietly enough.

“I understand that you’re going to be the principal witness for the Crown at my son’s trial?”

Ivy began to cry.

“Yes,” she sobbed. “Isn’t it dreadful﻿—dreadful? As if I hadn’t gone through enough without having to go through that too!”

===Chinese===

于是，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲向站在她面前的这个女人——她儿子爱到身败名裂的这个女人——发出了一声绝望的哀恳，尽管她把要说的话说得相当平静。

「我听说，在我儿子的审判庭上，您要做控方的头号证人？」

艾薇哭了起来。

「是啊，」她抽噎着说。「这不可怕么——太可怕了？我受的罪仿佛还不够，竟还要再加上这一桩！」

===Original===

“On what you say,” went on Mrs. Gretorex firmly, “may depend Roger’s life or death. After all, you and he were dear friends?”

She uttered that last sentence in a tone she strove to make conciliatory.

Ivy stopped crying. Then Roger hadn’t given her away, even to a very little extent, to his mother? It was a great relief to know that.

“I implore you to guard your tongue when you are in the witness-box,” went on Mrs. Gretorex.

===Chinese===

「罗杰是生是死，」格雷托雷克斯太太语气坚决地接着说，「也许就系在您说出口的话上。说到底，您和他到底是亲密的朋友呀？」

末了那句话，她说得竭力委婉，想求个缓和。

艾薇止住了哭。这么说，罗杰并没有出卖她——哪怕对他母亲，也丝毫没有？知道了这一点，真叫她如释重负。

「我恳求您，上了证人席（witness-box），千万看住您自己的舌头，」格雷托雷克斯太太继续说。

===Original===

“I will! I will indeed﻿—”

“Can you think of no natural explanation with regard to the utterly mysterious thing which happened?”

Her eyes were fixed imploringly on the beautiful little face of this frivolous﻿—Mrs. Gretorex believed mindless﻿—woman, whom Roger still loved so desperately.

“I’ve thought, and thought, and thought﻿—” whispered Ivy.

And then for the fourth time during this brief interview she uttered the words, “It’s all so strange.”

===Chinese===

「我会的！我真的会——」

「对这桩扑朔迷离到极点的事，您可想得出什么说得通的解释来么？」

她的眼睛恳求似的盯住这张美丽的小脸——这张轻浮的（格雷托雷克斯太太认定还是没脑子的）脸——这张脸的主人，罗杰至今还爱得那样死去活来。

「我想了，想了，又想——」艾薇喃喃道。

接着，在这场短短的会面里，她第四次说出了那句话：「这一切太蹊跷了。」

---

===Original===

As, a few minutes later, she walked down Kensington High Street, still full of bustling, happy people on shopping intent, Roger Gretorex’s mother was in an agony of doubt, wondering whether she had done well or ill in thus forcing herself on Mrs. Jervis Lexton.

Again and again there echoed in her ear the silly, vulgar little phrase: “Roger was so fearfully gone on me.”

Gone on her? Alas, that had been, that was still, only too true. Even now his one thought seemed to be how to spare Ivy pain, and, above all, disgrace.

She stepped up into a crowded omnibus at the corner of Chapel Street, and for a while she had to stand. Then a girl gave up her seat to her, and heavily she sat down.

===Chinese===

几分钟后，她走在肯辛顿大街上。街上依然满是脚步匆匆、欢欢喜喜的购物人潮。罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲心中疑虑如焚，不知自己这样硬闯到杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太面前，究竟是做对了还是做错了。

「罗杰又是那样死心塌地地迷着我」——这句愚蠢而俗气的小话，在她耳中一遍又一遍地回响。

迷着她？唉，这话从前不假，如今也还是不假，太不假了。直到现在，他念念不忘的好像仍是怎样免掉艾薇的痛苦，尤其是，免掉她的耻辱。

在查珀尔街（Chapel Street）街角，她登上一辆拥挤的公共汽车，有一程只好站着。后来一个姑娘给她让了座，她才沉沉地坐了下去。

===Original===

Who, looking however closely at Mrs. Gretorex sitting there, her worn face calm and still, would have thought her other than an old-fashioned, highly bred lady, leading the placid life of her fortunate class, that class which even now is financially secure, and seems to be so far apart from and above the sordid ills and anxieties of ordinary humanity?

Yet there can be little doubt that Roger Gretorex’s mother was the most miserable and the most unhappy woman of the many miserable and unhappy women in London that night. To the anguish, which was now her perpetual lot, was added a feeling that she had done, if anything, harm, in forcing herself on Mrs. Lexton.

“I’ve done no good!” she exclaimed as she walked into the sitting-room of the lodgings in Ebury Street where she and Enid Dent had taken refuge, after spending two or three days with a kind friend who, they had soon discovered though no word had been said, considered Roger almost certainly guilty.

===Chinese===

谁若细看她坐在那里的模样——那张憔悴的脸安详平静——能不当她是一位旧式的、教养极好的夫人，过着她们那个幸运阶级的安稳日子？那个阶级直到今日依然家底殷实，仿佛远远超脱于、也凌驾于凡夫俗子的腌臜病痛与忧愁之上。

然而几乎无可怀疑的是，那天夜里，在伦敦许许多多愁苦不幸的女人中间，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲是最愁苦、最不幸的一个。在她如今日日份内的煎熬之外，又添了一层感觉：她此番硬闯到莱克斯顿太太面前，若有何所得，所得的也只是害处。

「我白跑了一趟！」她一走进伊伯里街（Ebury Street）那处租住寓所的起居室便喊道。她和伊妮德·登特先在一位好心的朋友家耽了两三天——虽则谁都没有明言，她们很快便觉察出，那位朋友认定罗杰几乎必是罪人——之后，才躲到这里安身。

===Original===

The girl looked dismayed, for it had been at her suggestion that Mrs. Gretorex had gone to Duke of Kent Mansion.

Enid Dent now felt convinced that Ivy Lexton held the key to the mystery of Jervis Lexton’s death. She had never seen this woman whom she now knew that Roger loved, but she had formed a fairly clear and true impression of Ivy’s nature and character. Hatred, as well as love, has sometimes the power of tearing asunder the most skilfully woven web of lies.

And then there began for them all what seemed an interminable time of waiting. And all those nearly concerned with the case, apart from Ivy herself, felt almost a sense of relief when the winter day at last dawned which was to see Roger Gretorex stand his trial at the Old Bailey.

===Chinese===

姑娘露出了沮丧的神色，因为劝格雷托雷克斯太太去肯特公爵大厦的，正是她。

伊妮德·登特此刻深信，杰维斯·莱克斯顿之死的谜底，钥匙就握在艾薇·莱克斯顿手里。这个她如今知道罗杰爱着的女人，她从未见过，可她对艾薇的天性与为人，早已形成了相当清晰、相当真切的印象。恨，同爱一样，有时也自有一种力量，能把手艺再巧的谎言织成的罗网撕个粉碎。

于是，对他们所有人来说，开始了一段仿佛望不到头的等待。当那个冬日终于破晓——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯将在老贝利出庭受审的日子终于到来时，凡与本案切身相关的人，除了艾薇本人，几乎都觉出了一阵如释重负。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===

And now there were but two days﻿—to be accurate, but two nights﻿—to the date fixed for Roger Gretorex’s execution. All those to whom the matter was of grave moment had given up hope, and, to the great relief of each member of what may be described as the outside circle of those concerned in the still mysterious story, there was something stoic in the resignation and self-control of both the mother and the son.

But Enid Dent showed many signs of the strain and agony she was enduring, and Sir Joseph Molloy felt quite unlike his powerful, jovial self. Even against his better judgment he still felt convinced that an awful miscarriage of justice was going to be enacted. His conviction actually affected the nerves of Sir Edward Law, the Home Secretary, who happened, unfortunately for himself, to be one of Sir Joseph’s oldest friends.

Sir Edward hoped most fervently that Gretorex would make a last-moment confession. Not that the Home Secretary really doubted the fact of the young man’s guilt. But he felt the kind of anxiety which must possess any sensitive, conscientious human being who has the onerous gift of life in his hand, if he knows that the mind of a friend he trusts as deeply as he did trust Sir Joseph’s powerful mind, is convinced of a condemned man’s innocence.

The execution had been fixed for a Thursday. Deliberately Sir Joseph arranged to cross to Calais on the Wednesday in order to meet his wife, who was coming back the next day from the south of France.

Though Lady Molloy was an invalid, a tiny, fragile little body, and no longer young, her husband adored her, and when he was parted from “the woman who owned him,” as he sometimes oddly expressed it, he seemed at times only half himself. His Eileen, so he told himself now, would know how to heal the ache at his heart.

For one thing, Lady Molloy was deeply religious in a happy childlike way. Heaven seemed to her a beautiful place, just hard by, and so she would naturally view Roger Gretorex’s terrible mode of exit from life as the certain gateway to a happier existence than he could have hoped for on this earth.

===Chinese===

到了这时，距罗杰·格雷托雷克斯行刑的日子只剩两天——确切地说，只剩两夜了。凡与此事有重大干系的人，都已放弃了希望；而在这个至今仍扑朔迷离的故事里，那些堪称「外围中人」的相关者，见母子二人那般听天由命、克己自持，其中竟有一种坚忍恬淡的气度，人人心头都为之一宽。

然而伊妮德·登特身上，处处显出她正承受着的煎熬与痛楚；约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士也全然不似平素那副精力弥满、谈笑风生的样子。纵然违着自己的理性判断，他依旧坚信，一场可怕的冤狱即将坐实。他这份笃信，竟搅动了内政大臣爱德华·劳爵士（Sir Edward Law）的神经——不巧的是，这位大臣恰是约瑟夫爵士最老的朋友之一。

爱德华爵士满心恳切，只盼格雷托雷克斯能在最后关头招认。倒不是这位内政大臣当真怀疑这年轻人有罪。只是他体会到一种焦灼——凡是手握生杀大权（那实在是份沉重得可怕的赋予）、心思敏锐而良心未泯的人，只要得知一位自己深深信赖的朋友——他正是这样信赖着约瑟夫爵士那副强有力的头脑——竟认定一个待决之囚无辜，便必定会被这种焦灼攫住。

行刑定在星期四。约瑟夫爵士有意安排星期三渡海去加来（Calais），去接他的妻子——她次日就要从法国南部回来了。

莫洛伊夫人（Lady Molloy）虽是个久病之人，身量娇小纤弱，也不再年轻，丈夫却宠爱她至深；每逢与她——照他有时颇为古怪的说法，「那个拥有他的女人」——分开，他整个人便时有魂不守舍之态。他此刻对自己说，他的艾琳（Eileen）自会有法子抚平他心头的隐痛。

其一，莫洛伊夫人的信教虔诚，是那种快活而孩子气的虔诚。在她看来，天堂便是一个美丽的地方，近在咫尺；因此，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯那可怕的人生退场方式，在她眼里自然而然便是必经之门——通向一种比他在尘世间所能指望的更为幸福的存在。

---

===Original===

“The day after tomorrow﻿—the day after tomorrow.”

Those four words seemed to beat themselves on Enid Dent’s brain. Sometimes would come a variant﻿—“What can be done, surely something can be done, before the day after tomorrow?”

Early on the Tuesday morning she wandered out of doors and walked for miles in the cold, still empty streets. At last she went into Westminster Abbey for a while, and then into the vast Catholic Cathedral. But she found she could not pray. She felt as if abandoned by God, as well as by man.

Reluctantly, she at last turned her feet towards Ebury Street. She shrank from seeing Mrs. Gretorex. To Enid there was something horribly unnatural in the calmness and appearance of strength shown by Roger’s mother. It was, as the girl knew, by Roger’s plainly expressed desire that they were going home tomorrow down to Sussex. By his wish, also, they would be in the parish church, which was actually an enclave in the grounds attached to what was still his house, when nine o’clock struck out his hour of doom on Thursday.

===Chinese===

「后天——后天。」

这四个字仿佛一下一下敲在伊妮德·登特的心上。有时又会变个花样——「还能做点什么？后天之前，总归还能做点什么吧？」

星期二一大早，她信步走出门去，在清寒空寂、尚无人迹的街上走了好几英里。最后她进威斯敏斯特大教堂（Westminster Abbey）待了一阵，又走进那座宏伟的天主教大教堂。可她发觉自己竟无法祈祷。她只觉得自己被上帝抛弃了，一如被人抛弃。

她百般不情愿，最后还是挪步往伊伯里街去了。她怕见格雷托雷克斯太太。在伊妮德看来，罗杰的母亲显露出的那份平静与貌似坚强，有一种令人心头发毛的不自然。姑娘知道，正是应罗杰明白表示的心愿，她们明天就要回萨塞克斯乡下去。也是依着他的意思，星期四九点钟敲响他命定之时，她们将待在教区教堂里——那教堂恰好嵌在仍归于他名下的宅邸园地之内，自成一方圣域。

===Original===

As Enid came in to their sitting-room Mrs. Gretorex held out an open letter.

“This has just reached me﻿—sent on from Mr. Oram’s office this morning. It’s from the old woman who used to look after Roger.” And as the other took it from her, “Rather a touching letter, but I don’t feel I can bring myself to go to Ferry Place, my dear. I went there once, and spent such a happy, happy day with Roger. This Mrs. Huntley waited on us. Perhaps you will go instead of me? See what she says.”

Enid took the letter, and this is what she read:

===Chinese===

伊妮德走进她们的起居室时，格雷托雷克斯太太递过来一封已拆开的信。

「这信刚到我手里——今早从奥拉姆先生的事务所转来的。是那位从前照料罗杰的老妇人写来的。」待对方从她手里接过信，她又道：「信写得颇动人，可我实在打不起精神去费里街，亲爱的。我去过那儿一回，跟罗杰度过了那么快活、那么快活的一天。就是这位亨特利太太伺候我们的。也许你替我去一趟？看看她说什么。」

伊妮德接过信，读到的是这样一封：

===Original===

> Dear Madam,
>
> I don’t know what to do about the doctor’s things, and I should be glad if you could spare time to come here. Also there is something on my mind that I’d like to tell you, Mrs. Gretorex. But I gave my word, I even swore my oath to the doctor not to. So perhaps I oughtn’t to.
>
> I’ve tried to keep everything tidy, but the police pulled everything about so.
>
> Yours respectfully,
>
> Bertha Huntley.

===Chinese===

> 尊敬的夫人：
> 医生留下的东西我不知道该怎么处置，您若能抽空来此一趟，我将很高兴。另外，我心里有一件事想告诉您，格雷托雷克斯太太。可我答应过医生，甚至还对他起过誓，不说出去。所以也许我不该说。
> 我一直尽力把每样东西都收拾整齐，可警察把东西翻得乱七八糟。
> 您恭敬的仆人，
> 伯莎·亨特利（Bertha Huntley）。

===Original===

“I wonder what she wants to tell you?”

“I can form a shrewd guess,” said Mrs. Gretorex in a low voice.

The girl looked at her with eager eyes. “It may be something tremendously important,” she exclaimed.

The older woman shook her head.

“It might have had a certain importance before the trial, but it would have no importance now. I have no doubt that what Mrs. Huntley wants to tell me is﻿—”

Then she hesitated, for Mrs. Gretorex was an old-fashioned gentlewoman, who considered that certain things which unfortunately do happen in life should not be dwelt on, much less mentioned, by “nice” women.

===Chinese===

「不知她想告诉你什么？」

「我心里能猜个八九不离十。」格雷托雷克斯太太低声说。

姑娘热切地望着她。「那也许是天大了不得的大事。」她叫道。

年长的妇人摇了摇头。

「开庭之前，它或许还有几分分量；如今已毫无干系了。亨特利太太想告诉我的，无疑就是——」

说到这里她迟疑了，因为格雷托雷克斯太太是位旧式的大家闺秀，认为生活中不幸确会发生的一些事情，「体面」女人不该萦怀于心，更不该宣之于口。

===Original===

“What do you mean, Mrs. Gretorex? Do tell me!”

The girl was looking at her with perplexed, unhappy eyes. Perhaps, after all, it would be better to tell her the truth? It might cause her to forget Roger more quickly than she could otherwise do.

“I have very little doubt, Enid, that Mrs. Lexton, at one time, often went to Ferry Place. Naturally Roger bound the old woman to silence. He may have even made her swear that she would never reveal a fact so damaging to Ivy Lexton’s reputation. I don’t know if a knowledge of what I feel sure was the truth would have made any difference, one way or the other, at the trial. In any case, it won’t make any difference now.”

“May I go off to Ferry Place now?” asked Enid eagerly.

“Do, if you like. But be careful what you say, child.” She gazed into the girl’s flushed face. “I think we ought to do what we know Roger would wish us to do﻿—and not to do.”

And then, with a slight break in her even voice, she quoted the fine line﻿—“For silence is most noble to the end.”

===Chinese===

「您是什么意思，格雷托雷克斯太太？快告诉我吧！」

姑娘正用困惑而忧伤的眼睛望着她。说到底，还是把实话告诉她为好？实话或许能让她把罗杰忘得快些——不然的话，她是忘不掉的。

「伊妮德，我几乎可以断定，莱克斯顿太太有一阵子常去费里街。罗杰自然要这老妇人守口如瓶。他甚至可能要她起过誓，永远不泄露这样一桩有损艾薇·莱克斯顿名声的事。我不晓得，法庭上若是知道了这个我确信是事实的真相，会不会有什么不同——不管往哪边偏。事到如今，横竖是不会有什么不同了。」

「我现在可以去费里街一趟吗？」伊妮德急切地问。

「去吧，要是你愿意。可说话要留神，孩子。」她凝视着姑娘那张涨红的脸。「我想，凡是我们知道罗杰盼我们做的，就该去做；凡是知道他不盼我们做的，就不该做。」

随后，她那平和的嗓音微微一颤，引出那句佳句——「沉默至终，最为高贵。」

---

===Original===

As Enid Dent walked with what, to one passing her, would have appeared to be the happy, eager steps of youth towards Ferry Place, she more than once felt strongly inclined to turn back.

The thought of going to the house where Roger Gretorex had lived and worked during the months when he and she had become so entirely estranged was bitter to her. Also, she now had to endure the incessant talking and the kindly meant, but to her almost intolerable, sympathy of the landlady of Mrs. Gretorex’s lodgings. The thought that she would now endure more sympathy, and more garrulous talk, on the part of Mrs. Huntley was well nigh unendurable. Why not go back and write a nice letter to the old woman, explaining that Mrs. Gretorex was ill, but wished Mrs. Huntley to know how deep was her gratitude for everything she had done for her dear son?

And then, just as she was going to turn around, Enid felt ashamed of her strained nerves. If this old woman had been fond of Roger, then she must be very unhappy now.

She had to ask the way twice to Ferry Place, and each time she asked the question she saw a peculiar look come over the stranger’s face, showing, plainly enough, that he had recalled the fact that this was where Roger Gretorex had lived, the man who had committed murder for the sake of the woman he loved. The name of the obscure thoroughfare had been constantly mentioned, bandied to and fro, during Gretorex’s trial.

Enid soon found the double row of shabby little houses. It was strange to remember that Roger had lived for over a year in this sordid-looking place.

===Chinese===

伊妮德·登特朝费里街走去，那步子在一个路人眼里，想必显得是青春的、轻快而热切的步子；可她自己一路上不止一次，极想转身回去。

一想到要走进那座房子——在她与罗杰·格雷托雷克斯彻底疏远的那些月份里，他曾在那里生活、行医——她心里就发苦。再说，方才她还得忍受格雷托雷克斯太太寓所那位房东太太絮絮不休的谈话，以及那番出于好意、于她却几乎无法消受的怜悯。如今又要去领受亨特利太太更多的怜悯、更多的絮叨，这念头简直叫人忍无可忍。何不回去给老妇人写封得体的信，说明格雷托雷克斯太太病了，但愿亨特利太太知道，她为她爱子所做的一切，她是何等深深感念？

可就在她打算转身的一刻，伊妮德为自己这紧绷的神经感到羞愧。这位老妇人既然疼爱过罗杰，此刻必定伤心得很。

去费里街的路她得问上两回；每问一次，她都看见陌生人脸上掠过一种异样的神色，那分明表明对方想起了一个事实：这里正是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯——那个为了所爱的女人而犯下谋杀罪的男人——住过的地方。这条无名小街的街名，在格雷托雷克斯受审期间曾被反复提及，翻来覆去地念叨。

伊妮德很快找到了那两排寒碜的小屋。想起罗杰竟在这个看去脏兮兮的地方住了一年多，实在叫人觉得怪异。

===Original===

She walked slowly down the middle of the roadway till she reached No. 6. It looked just a little cleaner and “better class” than the houses on each side of it.

She knocked, and the door was opened almost at once, revealing a grey-haired, sad-faced old woman, who, before the visitor could speak, said sharply, “You’ve made a mistake. No one lives here now.”

“I’ve come from Mrs. Gretorex,” said Enid in a low voice.

And then the door, which had been nearly closed in her face, was opened widely.

“Come in, miss. Come in, do!” and the old woman opened a door to the left, and showed the visitor into what had been Roger Gretorex’s consulting-room. It was bare and poor-looking, but the girl, with a stab of pain, saw at once a small piece of furniture which had always stood in what was still called “the day nursery” at Anchorford Hall.

===Chinese===

她沿着街心缓缓走去，一直走到6号门前。这房子看上去只比左右邻舍整洁些、「上等些」。

她叩了门，门几乎应声而开，露出一位灰头发、愁容满面的老妇人；不等来客开口，她便没好气地说：「你走错门了。这儿现在没人住。」

「我是格雷托雷克斯太太打发来的。」伊妮德低声说。

那扇险些当面合上的门，随即敞开了。

「进来吧，小姐。快请进来！」老妇人打开左手一扇门，把来客引进那间曾是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯诊察室（consulting-room）的屋子。屋内空落落的，一望便知清寒；可姑娘一阵锥心的痛楚，立刻认出一件小家具——那件家具，一向摆在安克福德府里至今仍被称作「日间育儿室」的那个房间里。

===Original===

“Mrs. Gretorex is ill, or she would have come herself. But she has given me a message for you, Mrs. Huntley. She wishes me to tell you how grateful﻿—how grateful﻿—”

And then all at once Enid Dent broke down, and burst into a storm of tears.

She had not so “let herself go,” at any rate not in the daytime, since the end of Roger Gretorex’s trial. But somehow now, with this stranger, she didn’t care. It was such a comfort to have a good cry, and something seemed to tell her that this sad, anxious-looking old woman would understand, and sympathise with, her grief.

Mrs. Huntley pushed the sobbing girl gently down into the worn leather armchair in which Gretorex would sometimes put a delicate-looking woman patient﻿—the sort of patient who did not care to go into the surgery.

“I suppose,” said Mrs. Huntley in a troubled voice, “that you was the doctor’s young lady, miss?”

===Chinese===

「格雷托雷克斯太太病了，不然她就亲自来了。可她托我给你带了话，亨特利太太。她要我对你说，她是多么感激——多么感激——」

说到这里，伊妮德·登特蓦地撑不住了，泪水滂沱而下。

自罗杰·格雷托雷克斯一案审理结束以来，她还不曾这样「放开了哭」——至少白天不曾。可此刻，对着这么一个陌生人，她竟什么都顾不得了。痛痛快快哭一场原是莫大的宽慰；况且冥冥中似有什么告诉她，这位愁苦焦虑的老妇人会懂得她的悲伤，也会同情她的悲伤。

亨特利太太轻轻把啜泣的姑娘按进那把磨旧的皮扶手椅——格雷托雷克斯从前有时会让某位看去娇弱的女病人坐这把椅子，就是那种不情愿进诊室就诊的病人。

「我琢磨着，」亨特利太太的声音里透着不安，「你从前就是大夫的那位心上人吧，小姐？」

===Original===

It somehow comforted Enid to hear those simple words, uttered in so quiet, if pitying a tone.

“I think I was,” she sobbed. “Indeed, I am sure I was﻿—though that was a long time ago, Mrs. Huntley.”

“I know,” came the low-toned answer. And the old woman did know, perhaps better than anyone else in the world, why Roger Gretorex had left off thinking of the girl who now sat, the picture of despair, before her.

Enid suddenly got up. She dabbed her eyes with her handkerchief.

“And now,” she said, “let me deliver the rest of my message, Mrs. Huntley. Mrs. Gretorex knows how good you were to her son, and she wants me to tell you that a little later on she would like you to come down to Anchorford, for she does want to see you.”

“Later on?” echoed the old woman in a strange voice. “But that would be too late, miss. I has to see Mrs. Gretorex today for it to be of any good. Can’t you take me to her? Not that I likes to leave the house alone. I never do leave it﻿—not since I got the message from Mr. Oram that I was to regard myself as caretaker, that is.”

===Chinese===

这几句朴素的话，用那样安静——纵然含着怜惜——的声调说出来，伊妮德听了，竟得了几分安慰。

「我想是的。」她抽泣着说。「真的，我肯定是——虽说那是很久以前的事了，亨特利太太。」

「我知道。」回答的声音低沉。这位老妇人也确实知道——也许比世上任何人都更清楚——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯为什么不再念着此刻坐在她面前、一副绝望模样的这位姑娘。

伊妮德霍地站起身，用手帕按了按眼睛。

「好了，」她说，「让我把带来的话捎完吧，亨特利太太。格雷托雷克斯太太知道你待她儿子有多好，她要我对你说，过些时候她想请你到安克福德来一趟，因为她实在想见见你。」

「过些时候？」老妇人应了一声，嗓音古怪。「那就太迟了，小姐。要顶用，我今儿个就得见着格雷托雷克斯太太。你不能带我去见她吗？倒不是我乐意把屋子撂下没人看。我从不离开这儿——自打奥拉姆先生捎话来、要我把自个儿当作看管人那天起，就没离开过。就是这么回事。」

===Original===

“I am afraid you can’t see Mrs. Gretorex today,” said Enid firmly. “But I’ll give her any message, and﻿—and you can trust me, Mrs. Huntley, you really can!”

“I wonder if I can? I wonder if I dare?”

“Have you anything to say that we don’t already know?” she asked.

“Yes, I have, miss. But in telling it I may be doing wrong.”

“D’you mean something about Dr. Gretorex? Something that might, even now, make a difference?”

===Chinese===

「恐怕你今天见不着格雷托雷克斯太太了。」伊妮德语气坚决。「不过有什么话，我都会捎给她；而且——而且你可以信得过我，亨特利太太，真的可以！」

「也不知我能不能信？也不知我敢不敢信？」

「你有什么要说的，是我们还不知道的吗？」她问。

「有，小姐，我有。可说出来，我兴许是在作孽。」

「你是说关于格雷托雷克斯医生的事？哪怕是现在，还可能扭转局面的事？」

===Original===

“I don’t know. I can’t tell. I fear me it may be too late.”

“Let me judge of that,” said Enid Dent.

She had become quiet, collected, though she was filled with a feeling of suspense and, she dared not call it “hope.”

“Shall I tell you?” said Mrs. Huntley as if asking herself the question. And then, all at once, she answered it, “Yes, surely I will!”

===Chinese===

「我不知道。我说不上。我只怕已经太迟了。」

「迟不迟，让我来判断。」伊妮德·登特说。

她已归于安静、镇定，尽管满心悬悬；她不敢把那称作「希望」。

「我说不说呢？」亨特利太太说，像是在问自己。跟着，她蓦地自己答了：「说，当然要说！」

---

===Original===

Alfred Finch was reading a copy of an old complicated will. But though he was trying to concentrate on the business in hand, he found his mind straying persistently to the prison cell where Roger Gretorex sat waiting for the morning of the day after tomorrow. For one thing, he had heard by a side wind that the warder who had Gretorex in his special charge believed him innocent, and this made a great impression on him. That warder had had charge of over thirty men condemned to death, and this was the first time he had ever believed one of them to have been innocent of the crime for which he was to suffer death.

The telephone bell at his elbow rang.

“Miss Dent is on the line, Mr. Finch. Can you speak to her? She says it’s very urgent.”

“Put her through at once.”

And then he heard an eager, quivering voice, “Is that Mr. Finch? Can you come at once, Mr. Finch, to 6 Ferry Place? I believe I’ve got some new evidence.”

“New evidence?”

===Chinese===

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇正在读一份旧日复杂遗嘱的抄本。他虽竭力把心思收拢到手头的正事上，思绪却总固执地飘向那间囚室——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯正坐在里面，等着后天清晨的到来。其一，他曾间接听得风声：专门看管格雷托雷克斯的那名看守相信他是无辜的；这给他留下了极深的印象。那名看守先后看管过三十多个死囚，这还是头一回，他相信其中一个是清白的，不该为他将要受死的罪名偿命。

手边的电话铃响了。

「登特小姐来电话，芬奇先生。您能接吗？她说十万火急。」

「马上接进来。」

随后他听见一个急切发颤的声音：「是芬奇先生吗？您能马上来一趟吗，芬奇先生，到费里街6号？我相信我拿到了一些新证据。」

「新证据？」

===Original===

Mr. Finch, though he was alone, shook his head. Had he not himself done everything that was in the power of mortal man to procure new evidence in the last three weeks, and had he not entirely failed?

“I don’t wish to say more over the telephone, but can you come now, at once, to take a statement from Dr. Gretorex’s day maid, Mrs. Huntley?”

Mrs. Huntley? Why, that was the old caretaker woman! He remembered distinctly reading over the record of her short, colourless, unimportant interview with Inspector Orpington.

Mrs. Huntley could have nothing new to say of the slightest value. Stop, though﻿—she probably knew certain facts which might have been regarded as greatly to Mrs. Lexton’s discredit, had they come out at the trial. Facts which would certainly have added pungency to Sir Joseph Molloy’s speech for the defence. But Mrs. Huntley could have nothing to reveal that could make any real difference, now, to the fate of Roger Gretorex.

However, if only because he had come to like and respect Mrs. Gretorex’s young friend, Mr. Finch made up his mind he would do what Enid Dent desired.

===Chinese===

芬奇先生虽是独自一人，还是摇了摇头。过去这三个星期，凡人力之所及，他哪一样没有做尽，去寻新证据？可他不是一败涂地了吗？

「我不愿在电话里多说，可您能不能现在、马上就来，给格雷托雷克斯医生的日班女仆亨特利太太录一份证词？」

亨特利太太？嗐，那不就是看管屋子的那个老妇人嘛！他清楚地记得，自己逐字读过奥平顿探长对她那次简短、平淡、无关紧要的问话笔录。

亨特利太太不可能说出任何有丝毫价值的新东西。不过且慢——她多半知道某些事实，倘若在庭上抖出来，本会被看作大大有损莱克斯顿太太的名誉。某些事实，本必定会为约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士的辩方陈词平添几许辛辣。可亨特利太太不可能揭出什么能真正改变罗杰·格雷托雷克斯命运的东西——事到如今。

然而，哪怕只是因为他已喜欢上并敬重格雷托雷克斯太太的这位年轻朋友，芬奇先生也打定了主意，要照伊妮德·登特的心愿去做。

===Original===

“I’ll be with you within twenty minutes,” he called out.

“Be as quick as you can. I’m so frightened, Mr. Finch.”

“Frightened?” he repeated, surprised.

“Yes.” The voice dropped. “Supposing Mrs. Huntley were to die, suddenly, before you’ve heard what she’s got to say? I dare not tell you what it is over the telephone. But it is very important﻿—”

===Chinese===

「二十分钟之内我就到。」他扬声道。

「越快越好。我怕得很，芬奇先生。」

「怕？」他讶异地重复了一遍。

「嗯。」声音低了下去。「万一亨特利太太还没来得及说她要说的话，就突然死了呢？电话里我不敢说那是什么。可那非常重要——」

===Original===

Now Mr. Finch thought so little of what he was going to do, and, presumably, hear, that he simply left word for Mr. Oram that he had had to go out. And when he reached Westminster, he did not dismiss his taxicab; he left it at the end of Ferry Place.

Enid Dent stood waiting for him at the open door of the little house, and he noted at once the strained, excited look on her face.

Had she been a young man, and not a young woman, Alfred Finch would have exclaimed, “Come, come! What’s all this pother about?” But as it was, he looked at her very kindly, and made up his mind that he would “let her down” as gently as might be.

“I hope she’ll tell you all she told me,” murmured Enid as he shut the door. “Mrs. Lexton told a lie when she said she had never been here but once, and then with a woman friend. Mrs. Huntley swore to Roger Gretorex that she would say nothing about that, and she feels that she is breaking her oath. But I doubt if she realises herself the fearful importance of something else she told me, something Roger may suspect, but which only she actually saw.”

And then she opened the door of the consulting-room.

===Chinese===

芬奇先生对自己此行将做、大概也将听到的事情，全没放在心上，以致只给奥拉姆先生留了句话，说自己不得不出去一趟。到了威斯敏斯特，他也没打发掉出租汽车，而是让车停在费里街街口等着。

伊妮德·登特站在小屋敞开的门口等他；他一眼便注意到她脸上那种绷紧而亢奋的神情。

她若是个年轻人——年轻的男人，而非年轻的女人——阿尔弗雷德·芬奇准会嚷出声来：「得了，得了！这般慌慌张张，闹的哪一出？」可眼前既然不是，他便只朝她投去十分和善的目光，打定主意要把「让她失望」这件事，做得尽量温柔。

「但愿她会把告诉我的都告诉你。」他关上门时，伊妮德低声说。「莱克斯顿太太撒了谎——她说她只来过这儿一回，还是跟一位女朋友一起来的。亨特利太太向罗杰·格雷托雷克斯起过誓，对此只字不提；她如今觉得自己是在毁弃誓言。可她告诉我的另一件事有多可怕的要紧，我怀疑她自己都没意识到——那件事罗杰兴许猜得到，但真正亲眼见过的，只有她一个。」

随后她推开了诊察室的门。

===Original===

Mrs. Huntley was sitting all in a heap in a chair, staring before her. She looked up when the two came in, but she did not get up.

“Here is Mr. Finch. I want you to tell him exactly what you told me.”

“You tell him, miss,” muttered the old woman. “I’ve told you everything and﻿—and I feels very upset.”

“Mrs. Huntley is ready to swear,” said Enid quietly, “that she once found Mrs. Lexton alone in the surgery here, with a jar labelled arsenic standing on the table before her.”

Alfred Finch, startled, looked hard at the old woman. Was she telling the truth, or had she invented this ingenious story?

===Chinese===

亨特利太太瘫坐在一把椅子里，直勾勾望着前方。两人进来时她抬起眼，却没有起身。

「这位是芬奇先生。我要你把告诉我的话，原原本本告诉他。」

「你替我说吧，小姐。」老妇人咕哝道。「我都告诉你了，可——可我心里乱得很。」

「亨特利太太愿意起誓，」伊妮德平静地说，「她曾有一次撞见莱克斯顿太太独自待在这间诊室里，面前的桌上立着一只贴着砒霜标签的罐子。」

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇吃了一惊，紧紧盯着老妇人。她说的是实话，还是凭空编出了这么一段巧妙的故事？

===Original===

“When did that happen?” he asked quietly. “Is there any way in which you can fix the date of that occurrence, Mrs. Huntley?”

She looked up at him. “Yes,” she said dully. “ ’Twas the last time Mrs. Lexton ever had supper here. The doctor got a messenger boy, and sent him up to a grand shop in Piccadilly for some cold fish﻿—sole, I thinks it was﻿—done up in a newfangled fashion. Also there was a game pie, likewise an ice.”

“But how does that fix the date in your mind?” asked Alfred Finch rather impatiently.

“I can’t fix it. But you could, sir, from the messenger boys’ office. I heard one of them boys once tell the doctor that they kep’ all their receipts. ’Twas early last summer when that happened.”

He felt suddenly convinced that she at least believed she was telling the truth.

===Chinese===

「那是什么时候的事？」他平静地问。「有没有什么法子，能把那件事的日子确定下来，亨特利太太？」

她抬眼望他。「能。」她木然地说。「那是莱克斯顿太太末一回在这儿吃夜宵。大夫唤了个送信的信差少年（messenger boy），打发他去皮卡迪利一家高级铺子买冷鱼——我记得是鳎鱼——用新式花样装点过的。另外还有一只野味派，还有一道冰淇淋。」

「可这怎么就把日子定在你心里了？」阿尔弗雷德·芬奇问，颇有点不耐烦。

「我定不了。可您能啊，先生——去那家信差行查。我听见其中一个孩子有回跟大夫说，他们那些单据都留着呢。那事儿出在去年初夏。」

他蓦地信了：至少她自己相信，说的句句是实话。

===Original===

“The last time Mrs. Lexton had supper here?” It was that statement which in a sense impressed him. And had he been another kind of man he would undoubtedly have explained, “But you yourself signed a statement declaring that Mrs. Lexton had never been here, at 6 Ferry Place, excepting on one occasion to tea?”

But, instead of saying that, he observed encouragingly, “Now listen to me, Mrs. Huntley. You say, I notice, ‘the last time.’ Would Mrs. Lexton have been here to supper as many, say, as three or four times?”

“Much oftener than that!” exclaimed the old woman, rousing herself. “At one time, Mrs. Lexton was here constant. She’d come in just for ten minutes. ’Nother time, maybe, for a couple of hours. She’d phone first, to see if the doctor’ud be in. Mostly I couldn’t help knowing about it, though the doctor always made an excuse to get me out of the place before she come.”

“And on that last occasion, what exactly was it that happened? Are you sure this jar of arsenic was on the table, in front of Mrs. Lexton?”

In his eagerness he came and flung himself across a chair, close to the old woman.

===Chinese===

「莱克斯顿太太末一回在这儿吃夜宵？」在某种意义上，打动他的正是这句话。他若是另一种人，必定会指出来：「可你自己签过一份书面声明，说莱克斯顿太太除了有一回来喝过茶，从不曾到过费里街6号这地方？」

可他没这么说，而是鼓励道：「现在听我说，亨特利太太。我注意到，你说的是『末一回』。照你说，莱克斯顿太太来这儿吃夜宵，总有——比方说——三四回吧？」

「哪止那么多！」老妇人打起精神叫道。「有一阵子，莱克斯顿太太来得可勤了。有时进来只待十分钟；隔一回呢，兴许待上两个钟头。她总先打电话，问问大夫在不在家。多半我想不知道都不成——虽说每回她来之前，大夫总要寻个由头把我支出门去。」

「那么末回那次，究竟出了什么事？你敢肯定那罐砒霜就摆在桌上、摆在莱克斯顿太太面前？」

他一时心切，走过来横身坐上一把椅子，紧挨着老妇人。

===Original===

“I’m sure ’twas there, though I don’t know how it come there, excepting that the doctor had maybe some medicine to make up. I come in to clear up, and as I puts my key in the surgery door﻿—that’s our back-way in, sir﻿—she didn’t hear me. When she did, she was awfully put about. I begged her pardon, and I went away. And when I come round to the front of the house, I saw the doctor letting a man out. That was why Mrs. Lexton was alone, then, in the surgery. She was waiting for the doctor, maybe to get her a cab. He often did that.”

“I suppose you can give me nothing that would afford any corroboration as to what you have just told me? I mean that would make anyone know that you are now telling the truth? *I* believe you, Mrs. Huntley, but you know that, in a matter of this sort, belief doesn’t go very far. People want proof.”

“I knows that. But I can’t say no more than I have said.”

“Is there nothing? Think, Mrs. Huntley!” exclaimed Enid Dent. “Did you never tell anyone outside that you’d found Mrs. Lexton in the surgery under such curious circumstances?”

===Chinese===

「我敢肯定罐子是在那儿，虽说我不晓得它怎么会搁在那儿——除非是大夫兴许要配点什么药。我是进来收拾屋子的；我把钥匙插进诊室门上——那是我们打后头进来的门，先生——她没听见。等她瞧见了，可是慌得不行。我给她赔了不是，就走开了。等我绕到房子前头，看见大夫正送一位先生出去。所以那会儿莱克斯顿太太才一个人在诊室里。她是在等大夫，兴许等他给她叫车。他常这么办的。」

「我想，关于你方才所说的，你拿不出任何可以佐证的东西吧？我是说，能让人确知你如今说的是实话的东西？*我*是信你的，亨特利太太，可你知道，在这类事情上，空口相信顶不了多大事。人们要的是证据。」

「这我知道。可除了说过的，我再说不出别的了。」

「当真没有了吗？想想，亨特利太太！」伊妮德·登特叫道。「你从没跟外头的任何人说过，你是在那样蹊跷的情形下撞见莱克斯顿太太在诊室里的吗？」

===Original===

“I give the doctor my word I’d never tell on either of them. He said I could do a great thing for him in doing that, and I’ve kep’ my word till today.”

And then Alfred Finch had something like an inspiration.

“Of course, I know the police made a thorough search of this house,” he observed. “But I ask myself, Mrs. Huntley, if they overlooked anything﻿—*anything in the way of a letter or letters*?” And he looked very hard at the old woman.

Mrs. Huntley blinked at him, and for the first time she looked uneasy and ashamed.

“I’ve got summat,” she said in a low reluctant voice. “Summat I’ve no business to have. It’s two love-letters Mrs. Lexton wrote to the doctor. As was his way, poor young gentleman, he tore them up in little pieces, and﻿—”

===Chinese===

「我向大夫发过话，永远不会告发他们俩当中的哪一个。他说，我这样做，等于帮了他天大的忙；我的话，我一直守到今天。」

就在这时，阿尔弗雷德·芬奇灵光一闪。

「我当然知道，警察把这宅子里里外外搜了个遍。」他说。「可我自个儿纳闷，亨特利太太，他们有没有漏掉什么——*比如一封信，或者几封信之类的什么东西*？」说着他死死盯着老妇人。

亨特利太太朝他眨巴着眼，头一回露出了不安和羞愧的神色。

「我藏着点东西。」她的声音低而勉强。「不该留的东西。是莱克斯顿太太写给大夫的两封情书。那位可怜的年轻老爷，照他惯来的做法，把信撕成了小碎片，后来——」

===Original===

“You pieced them together,” observed Mr. Finch pleasantly.

Enid Dent gave a gasp, as he went on:

“If you can produce those letters, I think I can promise you, Mrs. Huntley, that Dr. Gretorex will not hang the day after tomorrow. They, together with a sworn statement made by you before a Commissioner of Oaths, will provide what is called ‘new evidence.’ I want you to go with me now into the surgery, to tell me exactly where Mrs. Lexton was standing when you surprised her. You are sure that she was alone?” he added quickly.

“She was quite alone,” said Mrs. Huntley positively. “I come in softly like, and there she was! I can show you exactly where she was standing.”

===Chinese===

「你再把碎片拼了起来。」芬奇先生和悦地接了一句。

伊妮德·登特倒抽一口气，他继续说道：

「你若能拿出那两封信，我想我可以向你担保，亨特利太太，格雷托雷克斯医生后天不会被绞死。那两封信，连同你在宣誓公证人（Commissioner of Oaths）面前立下的一份宣誓书，便构成了所谓的『新证据』。我现在要你跟我到诊室里去，确切告诉我，你惊动她时莱克斯顿太太站的位置。你肯定当时只有她一个人？」他又急急补了一句。

「就她一个人，千真万确。」亨特利太太斩钉截铁地说。「我进去时轻手轻脚的，她就那么在那儿！她站的那个位置，我能一五一十指给你看。」

===Original===

She got up and led the way down the passage, and through the two doors which shut off the surgery from the house.

“Is everything here just as it was?” asked Mr. Finch quickly.

“No, sir. They took away everything as was in that cupboard, but they left the books.”

He glanced up at the row of shabby volumes in the hanging bookcase, but made no comment.

“Is that the same table where stood the jar labelled arsenic?”

===Chinese===

她站起身，领路走过过道，穿过那两道把诊室与住房隔开的门。

「这里的东西都还是原样吗？」芬奇先生急问。

「不是了，先生。橱柜里的东西他们都搬走了，书倒留下了。」

他抬眼看了看悬挂书架上那一排寒碜的书，未置一词。

「桌上立着砒霜罐的，就是这张桌子吗？」

===Original===

Mrs. Huntley put her work-worn hand on a certain spot on the deal table.

“The jar was here; the light was full on it, and I saw it plain as plain.”

And then she acted, or, rather, enacted, the scene with some spirit, making Enid Dent stand exactly where Ivy Lexton had stood.

“I noticed particular how she was dressed,” she went on eagerly. “She always dressed very dainty-like, lovely clothes they was! And she had the most peculiar looking bag I ever did see. ’Twas exactly like mother-of-pearl. Lovely it was! I noticed it when she turned round. Says she, ‘Why, Mrs. Huntley, how you did startle me,’ or something like that, sir.”

===Chinese===

亨特利太太把那双做活儿做粗了的手，按在松木桌的某一点上。

「罐子就在这儿；灯光正正照着它，我看得真真切切。」

随后她竟有模有样地把那一幕演了起来——也可以说是重演——让伊妮德·登特站到艾薇·莱克斯顿当年站过的确切位置上。

「她那天穿什么衣裳，我特别留意了。」她兴致勃勃地说下去。「她穿得总是那么讲究，那些衣裳多好看哪！还有她那只提包，是我这辈子见过的最特别的。活脱脱像是珍珠母做的。可好看啦！她一转身我就瞧见了。她说：『哎哟，亨特利太太，你可把我吓了一大跳，』还是句什么类似的话，先生。」

===Original===

Alfred Finch was writing down every word that came out of her mouth. He was one of those men who never lose a chance, and he had invented a kind of shorthand for himself. Everything the woman had said since he had come into the room had been put on record by him.

“And now,” he said quietly, “I’ll trouble you to show me those two letters.”

Finch noticed that Mrs. Huntley gave just an imperceptible glance towards the girl who stood a little aside, gazing into vacancy, as if her thoughts were far away, as indeed they were﻿—with Roger Gretorex in his prison cell.

“Yes, sir, I’ll go and get the letters, but I do hope Dr. Gretorex won’t ever know I did such a thing as that, sir? I was very attached to the doctor, and that made me feel curious, I suppose. I oughtn’t to have acted so, sir; I knew I was doing wrong.”

“All I can say now is, thank God you did do wrong, Mrs. Huntley! But don’t you worry﻿—we won’t let him ever know you did what you did. After all, anyone who found those pieces might have put them together, eh? Why people don’t burn compromising documents always beats me! I’ve got a cab at the end of the street, and I want you to come along this very minute to a Commissioner of Oaths. I’ve got all you’ve told me in black and white. You’ll only have just to repeat word for word what I’ve got down here before the gentleman, and then swear it’s true.”

===Chinese===

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇正把她口中说出的每一个字都记录下来。他是那种从不放过任何机会的人，还给自己发明了一套速记法。自他进屋以来，这女人说过的一切，都已被他录在案了。

「好了，」他平静地说，「麻烦你把那两封信拿给我看看。」

芬奇注意到，亨特利太太朝姑娘那边飞快地、几乎察觉不到地瞥了一眼；姑娘略站开些，目光茫然望着虚空，仿佛心思远在他方——也确实如此：她的心思正与罗杰·格雷托雷克斯一道，在那间囚室里。

「好，先生，我去把信取来。可我真盼着格雷托雷克斯医生永远不知道我干过这种事，先生？我打心眼里喜欢这位大夫，兴许就因为这个，才起了那份好奇心。我不该那样做，先生；我那时候也知道是在作孽。」

「如今我只能说：谢天谢地，幸亏你作了这个恶，亨特利太太！不过你放心——我们绝不会让他知道你干过什么。再说，谁捡着那些碎片，都可能把它们拼起来，对吧？人们干吗不把招祸的文书烧掉，这一直让我纳闷！街口我备着一部车，我要你这会儿就跟我去见一位宣誓公证人。你跟我说的这一切，我都白纸黑字记下了。到那位先生面前，你只消把我记在这里的话一字一句重说一遍，然后起誓属实就成。」

===Original===

“But do you think I ought to leave the house, sir?”

“We can leave Miss Dent here, while I go on to your place to get those letters.”

When, within a quarter of an hour, the three were standing outside the queer little office of a Commissioner of Oaths, with whom Alfred Finch happened to be acquainted, Mr. Finch said something which surprised Enid Dent. “I think you’d better not come in here with us,” he muttered. “You see, it’s better, in such a case, to have the witness alone. Prevents her being nervous.”

She did not guess the truth, which was that, in the few minutes he had been away with the old woman, she had spoken more freely than she had cared to do before the girl whom she regarded as Gretorex’s “young lady.” And some of the things she had then told him Alfred Finch determined should be embodied in her statutory declaration. Mr. Finch was keenly alive to the value of prejudice. He was aware that the Home Secretary was a man of rigid, some would have said too rigid, moral principle.

So it was with considerable satisfaction that he had exclaimed, after reading through the two letters, “My word! Mrs. Lexton’s what *I* call a hot cup of tea. Eh? Mrs. Huntley?”

Solemnly she had nodded her head. She had always known that such was the fact, though she wouldn’t perhaps have put it in just those words, for she was a refined, delicate-natured old woman.

===Chinese===

「可您觉得我该离开这屋子吗，先生？」

「登特小姐可以留在这儿，我先上你屋里去取那两封信。」

一刻钟之内，三个人已站在一位宣誓公证人的怪模怪样的小办公室门外——这位公证人恰是阿尔弗雷德·芬奇的熟人。芬奇先生说了一句让伊妮德·登特吃惊的话。「我看你最好别跟我们一起进去。」他低声说。「你要知道，遇上这种事，让证人独自一个更好。省得她紧张。」

她没有猜到实情：方才他与老妇人单独待着的那几分钟里，她说话比在那位被她视作格雷托雷克斯「心上人」的姑娘面前放得开多了。她那时告诉他的一些事情，阿尔弗雷德·芬奇打定主意，要写进她的法定声明（statutory declaration）里。芬奇先生对成见的分量，敏锐得很。他知道那位内政大臣是个道德原则严苛的人——有人甚至会说过于严苛。

于是，读完那两封信后，他心满意足地感叹道：「我的天！要说莱克斯顿太太嘛，照*我*看，可真是一杯滚烫的茶。嗯？亨特利太太？」

她郑重地点了点头。事实如此，她心里一向有数——只是她自己多半不会用那样的话来讲，因为她是个教养有素、心性细致的老妇人。

## XV / 十五

===Original===

During the night which preceded the day when Ivy Lexton was to appear as chief witness for the Crown, she lay awake, hour after hour, dreading with an awful dread the ordeal that lay before her.

Her chattering, excited circle of friends had all unwittingly terrified her with their accounts of how Gretorex’s counsel, Sir Joseph Molloy, was apt to deal with a witness. And in the watches of the night, Ivy, shivering, saw herself faced by that ruthless cross-examiner.

What was this formidable advocate going to say to her, to get out of her, by what one of her admirers had laughingly called “his exercise of the Third Degree?”

For the first time the widow of Jervis Lexton realised how insincere and how shallow were the sympathy and the cloying flattery with which she was now surrounded. Only two human beings seemed really sorry at the thought of what was going to happen to her tomorrow﻿—Lady Flora Desmond and Philip Paxton-Smith.

===Chinese===

在艾薇·莱克斯顿将以控方头号证人身份出庭的前一夜，她一小时又一小时地睁眼躺着，怀着一种骇人的恐惧，惧怕着横在她面前的那场考验。

她那一圈子叽叽喳喳、兴冲冲的朋友，无意之间把她吓了个不轻——他们绘声绘色地讲起格雷托雷克斯的出庭律师约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士（Sir Joseph Molloy）对付证人惯使的手段。于是在长夜的无眠时分，艾薇打着寒噤，仿佛看见自己正面对着那个冷酷无情的盘问者。

这位令人生畏的大律师会对她说些什么，又会从她嘴里逼出些什么——用的正是她的一个仰慕者戏称的「他的第三级审讯」（the Third Degree）？

杰维斯·莱克斯顿的遗孀平生第一次认识到，如今将她团团围住的那份同情、那甜得发腻的奉承，是何等虚伪、何等浅薄。想到明天将落到她头上的事，真正觉得难过的，似乎只有两个人——弗洛拉·德斯蒙德小姐和菲利普·帕克斯顿-史密斯。

===Original===

The concern manifested by her solicitor made Ivy feel sick with apprehension. He had spent hours with her trying to teach her what she had to say; that is, what to admit, what to deny, during her cross-examination.

It was plain, dreadfully plain, to her, that Paxton-Smith was very much afraid of how the great Sir Joseph Molloy would treat her when he had her in his power.

Again and again, during that long winter night, she asked herself with terror whether Sir Joseph could have found out anything with regard to her past relations with Roger Gretorex.

She knew Gretorex far too well to suppose, even for a moment, that he had given her away. But the short interview with Roger’s mother, though she, Ivy, had appeared to come out of it so well, had left a frightening impression. And she shivered as she recalled the terrible expression which had come over Mrs. Gretorex’s face when making to her the appeal which she had rejected with words implying that she, too, believed the man who loved her had been guilty of a terrible crime.

===Chinese===

律师所流露的关切，使艾薇忧虑得心里发呕。他曾一连几个钟头和她在一起，力图教会她该说些什么；也就是说，在受交叉盘问（cross-examination）时，什么该承认，什么该否认。

她看得明明白白——明明白白得可怕——帕克斯顿-史密斯深怕那位了不得的约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士一旦把她攥进掌心，不知会怎样作践她。

在那漫漫冬夜里，她一遍又一遍地惊惧自问：对于她与罗杰·格雷托雷克斯昔日的那段关系，约瑟夫爵士会不会已经查出了什么端倪。

她对格雷托雷克斯知之太深，绝不至于有一瞬间疑心是他出卖了她。可是同罗杰母亲的那次短晤，虽然她艾薇表面上应付得漂漂亮亮，却给她留下了一个骇人的印象。她一想起当时的情形便不寒而栗：格雷托雷克斯太太向她发出恳求时，脸上浮起的那副可怕神情——而她回绝时的那些话，无异于表明她自己也相信，这个爱她的男人确实犯下了一桩可怕的罪行。

===Original===

Ivy even asked herself with a kind of angry resentment, in the darkness of the night, why Roger Gretorex had not done this thing of which he stood accused?

Her own set, the men and women round her, all seemed to think it natural, in a sense, that he should have done it. And yet, though he had had many opportunities of ridding himself of Jervis Lexton, in the days when he had been so much with them, and though the only bar at one moment which had stood in the way of his happiness had been the life of Jervis Lexton, the thought of doing such a thing had evidently never even occurred to his mind!

Looking back, Ivy knew that there had been a time last winter when, had she then become a widow, she would have married Gretorex. She had been﻿—how curious to remember that time now, though it was less than a year ago﻿—infatuated with the splendid-looking young man who loved her with so intense and passionate a devotion.

She remembered, also, how reckless she had been in those old days. Anyone but Jervis would have suspected the truth. Thank God, she hadn’t known Miles Rushworth, even slightly, during those mad weeks of what she had called her love for Roger Gretorex. Rushworth would have guessed, nay more, he would have known, what was going on.

===Chinese===

在夜的黑暗里，艾薇甚至带着几分愠怒的委屈问自己：罗杰·格雷托雷克斯为什么就没有干下他被控的这桩事来呢？

她自己的那个圈子，她周围的男男女女，在某种意义上，似乎个个都觉得他干出这事来顺理成章。然而，在他同他们夫妻过从甚密的那些日子里，他本来有的是机会摆脱杰维斯·莱克斯顿；纵然一度横在他幸福路上的唯一障碍，就是杰维斯·莱克斯顿的性命，可干这种事的念头，显然压根儿就不曾在他的脑海里闪现过！

回首前尘，艾薇明白，去年冬天曾有那么一段时候，倘若她当时做了寡妇，她是会嫁给格雷托雷克斯的。她曾痴恋着这个仪表堂堂的青年——他爱她爱得那样炽烈、那样忘我——如今回想那一段时光，虽是不到一年前的事，却已觉得多么不可思议。

她还记得，那些日子里自己是何等胆大妄为。换了杰维斯以外的任何人，都会疑心到真相。谢天谢地，在她自称爱上罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的那几周疯狂日子里，她还压根儿不认识迈尔斯·拉什沃思。若是认识，拉什沃思准会猜到——岂止猜到，他准会知道——当时是怎么一回事。

===Original===

Had Roger’s mother suspected the truth? Almost certainly, yes. If Mrs. Gretorex thought it would help Roger, she would of course tell the famous advocate who was now fighting for her son’s life what she believed had been the real relations between her son and the woman who was to be the chief witness against him.

Always it was to Sir Joseph Molloy, the man whose name she had never heard till, say, a fortnight ago, that Ivy’s thoughts turned with dread, during those endless hours of darkness when she tossed this way and that through the long night.

Nurse Bradfield had had a terrible time in the witness-box. Indeed, she had confessed to Ivy last evening that Sir Joseph could have made her say black was white and wrong right! He had dwelt with sinister insistence on the short time that she, Mrs. Lexton, had been left alone with Mr. Lexton on that fatal last afternoon; nay, more, he had almost gone so far as to imply that, had Nurse Bradfield been faithful to her trust and had not gone out for those few minutes, Jervis Lexton might be alive today. Also he had called her “Woman!” She had even appealed to the Judge to protect her﻿—not that that had done her much good.

The nurse’s account of the ordeal she had been through filled Ivy with such foreboding that she would have done anything, even gone back to the old black days when she and Jervis lived in those poverty-stricken Pimlico lodgings, if she could thereby have wiped out all that had happened since.

===Chinese===

罗杰的母亲可曾疑心到真相？几乎可以断定，疑心过。倘若格雷托雷克斯太太认为这样做于罗杰有益，她自然会把儿子的真实关系——她所认定的儿子与那个即将出面指控他的头号女证人之间的真实关系——和盘托给那位如今正为他儿子性命奋战的赫赫有名的大律师。

在那无尽无休的黑夜时辰里，艾薇辗转反侧，心怀惧意，思来想去总离不了一个约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士——这个人名，直到大约两星期之前，她还从来不曾听人说起过。

布拉德菲尔德护士在证人席上捱过了一段可怕的时光。的确，昨天傍晚她向艾薇招认：约瑟夫爵士简直能叫她把黑说成白、把错说成对！他曾怀着阴森的执拗，死死咬住一点不放：在那致命的最后一个下午，她——莱克斯顿太太——被单独留下来与莱克斯顿先生相处的短短时刻；岂止如此，他几乎就要明说：倘若布拉德菲尔德护士恪尽职守、不曾出去那几分钟，杰维斯·莱克斯顿今天兴许还活着。他还唤她作「女人！」她甚至请求法官庇护她——不过那也没顶多大用。

护士讲述的这场磨难的经过，使艾薇满心生出不祥之兆。只要能把此后发生的一切一笔勾销，她什么都肯干，哪怕退回到从前那些暗无天日的日子——她和杰维斯挤在皮姆利科那几间寒酸公寓里度日的时候。

===Original===

When at last there came the morning, she got up, pale and really ill.
