Then she waited, in an extremity of nervous fear, till, at last, there came the moment when Mr. Paxton-Smith, looking, so she told herself, like an undertaker, and not at all like his usual jovial self, called in his car to take her to the Old Bailey.

During their long drive in the crowded streets the lawyer remained almost entirely silent. He had explained and made her rehearse yesterday, carefully and kindly, everything she must say and refrain from saying.

When passing the Piccadilly end of Bond Street, for the chauffeur, chauffeur-like, had taken them the longest way, a sob escaped Ivy. There had risen before her a vision of carefree, happy nights, spent in dancing, and in what old-fashioned people would have called riotous living, within a few yards of where the car was now being held up in the traffic.

===Chinese===

好不容易熬到天亮，她起了床，面色苍白，真像是病了。随后她便在紧张恐惧的极点中干等着，直到帕克斯顿-史密斯先生乘汽车来接她去老贝利的那一刻——她自己觉得，他那一副神情活像殡仪馆里的人，全然不是平日那个乐呵呵的样子了。

汽车穿行在拥挤的街头，一路漫漫，律师几乎始终一言不发。该说的、不该说的，他昨天已一一细心而和气地向她交代明白，又让她预演过一遍。

车子经过邦德街（Bond Street）临皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的一头——那司机一身司机的脾性，专拣最远的道走——艾薇不由得哽咽了一声。昔日夜夜欢歌的景象在她眼前浮起：就在此刻汽车被堵在车流里的那几码远的地方，她曾流连于舞池，过着老派人口中所说的花天酒地的生活。

===Original===

After they had gone on again, her frightened eyes caught glimpses of the newspaper placards. On each one was blazoned forth her now notorious name:

> Lexton mystery.

> Lexton murder.

> Nurse in the box.

Though she was singularly unimaginative, Ivy shuddered as she told herself that, in a couple of hours from now, maybe there would be the words, “Mrs. Lexton in the Box,” or, worse by far, “Mrs. Lexton Cross-examined.”

At last, after what seemed both to Paxton-Smith and to his client a long drive, the car drew up by a side door of the great frowning building called by that name of dread to every evildoer the Old Bailey.

===Chinese===

车子重新前行后，她那双惊惶的眼睛扫过一张张报馆招贴。每一张上面，都赫然印着她如今声名狼藉的姓氏：

> 莱克斯顿疑案。

> 莱克斯顿凶案。

> 护士上证人席。

艾薇这人虽毫无想象力，此刻也打了个寒噤。她对自己说：再过两个钟头，招贴上也许就会出现「莱克斯顿太太上证人席」的字样，或者，更糟得多的——「莱克斯顿太太受盘问」。

这趟在帕克斯顿-史密斯和他当事人看来都漫长得可以的行程总算到了头，汽车在那座愁云满面、气象威严的巨厦一扇边门外停了下来——这座大厦的名字，老贝利，天下作恶之徒闻之无不胆寒。

---

===Original===

What an awesome, and in some ways superb, spectacle is the scene presented by every British trial for murder! And if this is always true, even in the humblest country town Assize Court, how much more tense and awe-inspiring is what takes place in the courthouse of the Old Bailey, when the prisoner in the dock is the central figure in a murder mystery which has suddenly become world-famous. Especially is this true when the accused man is putting up a struggle, not only for his life, but what to some men really does mean more than life﻿—his honour.

Since the war there has appeared in London a new world of idle, luxury-loving human beings who live for pleasure, and who, if their income is fluctuating and uncertain, yet mysteriously appear always plentifully provided with ready money to burn on what they call “fun.”

To the eyes of those composing this new world, lovely Ivy Lexton, and good-humoured, popular Jervis Lexton, had been familiar figures, especially during the years when they were merrily engaged in running through their capital. All these people regarded the trial of Roger Gretorex as a spectacle produced and staked for their special benefit, and while the more enterprising and fortunate among them attended each day the exciting proceedings at the Old Bailey, the others all read with avid interest the full accounts of the trial which appeared every morning in whatever happened to be their favourite daily paper.

Although the case was called the Lexton Mystery, none of the hundreds of thousands of Roger Gretorex’s fellow-countrymen and countrywomen, who were following each detail of the story as unfolded now day by day in Court, considered that there was very much mystery about it. What was of tense interest, and what formed the real enigma, was the latest variant of the eternal triangle﻿—the story of the relations of the three, wife, husband, and lover.

===Chinese===

英国每一场谋杀案审判，呈现出来的是何等慑人、又在某种意义上何等壮观的场面！即便在最不起眼的乡间小镇的巡回法院（Assize Court）里，这一点也总是不假；那么，当老贝利法庭的被告席上站着的，是一桩骤然间名闻全球的谋杀疑案的中心人物时，那里的情景又该何等紧张、何等震慑人心。尤其是当被告拼力相争的不只是性命，还有对某些男人来说的确重于性命的东西——他的名誉时，更是如此。

自战争以来，伦敦出现了一个由游手好闲、耽于逸乐之徒组成的新世界。这些人活着就是为了行乐；收入纵然起伏不定、朝不保夕，他们却总能神秘地手头宽裕，大把现钱随取随用，去挥霍在他们所谓的「寻欢作乐」上。

在组成这个新世界的人们眼里，美丽的艾薇·莱克斯顿，和和气讨喜、人缘极好的杰维斯·莱克斯顿，都是熟面孔——尤其是在他们夫妻俩欢天喜地坐吃山空的那些年头。这班人全都把罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的审判看作一出专为他们排演、供他们下注的好戏：其中更有闯劲、运气也更佳的，每天亲临老贝利，旁观那激动人心的审案进程；其余的人，则每天早晨津津有味地细读各家心爱的日报上恰好刊登的审判详报。

这桩案子虽然名叫「莱克斯顿疑案」，可是千千万万追随着案情在法庭上逐日展开的罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的男女同胞，没有一个觉得它有多少「疑」可言。真正紧张有趣、构成真正谜团的，是永恒三角的最新变种——妻子、丈夫与情人三人关系的故事。

===Original===

One doubt remained in many a mind. That doubt concerned the relations of Ivy Lexton and of Roger Gretorex. To what extent, if any, had that beautiful young woman been involved in her lover’s guilt? Was it true that her own feelings, with regard to the young man who had slowly done a husband to death so that a wife should be free, had been simply those which it was known she was going into the witness-box to swear they had been? Had they really been feelings of kindly and indifferent, not to say tepid, friendship?

Another question which is always being asked by every student of human nature was asked in this case﻿—that is, whether a certain kind of exalted passion, the passionate love of a man for a woman which leads to crime, can exist without even a touch of secret encouragement?

The more worldly-wise shook their heads, and said that, whatever romantic poets and novelists may aver, such entirely unrequited passion on the part of an intelligent, educated man is impossible. Surely, before such a man as Roger Gretorex had set out to do that awful thing, he must, at any rate, have had some cause to believe that Ivy Lexton, when widowed, would become his wife?

There was yet another point which made this judicial drama appear, to use a phrase sometimes used in such a connection, “a full-dress trial.” Justice may be blind, yet she can see the glitter of gold. No money had been spared on either side. Indeed, judging by the array of counsel engaged, there must have been limitless wealth available for the defence. And, in a sense, there was, for Mrs. Gretorex had thrown all the fortune that remained to her into the struggle for her son’s life.

===Chinese===

在许多人心里，还存着一层疑惑。这疑惑关乎艾薇·莱克斯顿与罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的关系。那个美丽的年轻女人，到底在多大程度上——如果真有几分的话——被卷进了她情人的罪愆？那个青年为了一个妻子能得到自由，慢慢地下手弄死了她的丈夫；而她本人对他的感情，当真如人们所知她即将走进证人席宣誓的那样，只是那么一种感情吗？当真只是一种与人为善而无关痛痒——甚至可说是温吞淡漠——的友情吗？

每一个研究人性的人总要问的那个问题，在这桩案子里也被问了出来——那就是：某一种超凡入圣的炽情，一种男人对女人的、终于引向犯罪的爱恋，难道能够存在，而连一丝半缕暗中的鼓励都没有吗？

老于世故的人们摇着头说，任凭浪漫的诗人与小说家如何断言，一个有头脑、受过教育的男人，绝不可能怀有这种全然单方面的痴情。像罗杰·格雷托雷克斯这样的人，在下手去干那桩可怕的事之前，无论如何总该有几分理由相信：艾薇·莱克斯顿一旦守寡，是会嫁给他的吧？

还有一点，使这出司法大戏成了人们在此类场合偶尔用到的那句话所说的「一场大张旗鼓的审判」（a full-dress trial）。正义女神也许双目失明，却看得见金子的闪光。双方都不惜血本。单看辩护方延聘的出庭律师阵容，便可知供辩护支取的财力想必没有底。而在某种意义上，财力也确实没有底，因为格雷托雷克斯太太已把名下剩下的全部家财，都投进了这场为儿子性命而打的官司里。

---

===Original===

And now, on the fourth day, was approaching the great moment of Roger Gretorex’s trial for murder. The highest peak of the fever chart of this drama, which was being watched not only by those who were present in Court, but by hundreds of thousands of English-speaking people all over the world, was now about to be reached.

There came a peculiar rustle through the Court, followed by a moment of complete silence, as Ivy Lexton stepped, with short, dainty steps into the witness-box, and faced what appeared to her a myriad world of eyes fixed on her pale countenance. In accordance with a strong hint given yesterday by her solicitor, Ivy had not made up her face at all today.

Paxton-Smith, as the tense moments flew by, felt full of admiration for his beautiful client.

Ivy even remembered everything that he had advised with regard to her behaviour when in the witness-box, including certain things she might well have been pardoned for forgetting. One of these had been that, when answering counsel for the Crown, she should hold her head well up.

===Chinese===

而今，到了第四天，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯谋杀案审判的重大时刻临近了。这出戏的热度曲线，正要攀上最高峰——盯着这条曲线的，不光是到庭的旁听者，还有全世界千千万万讲英语的人。

艾薇·莱克斯顿迈着细碎轻盈的步子走进证人席，面对着在她看来如千眼世界般的一双双盯住她苍白面庞的眼睛，法庭里起了一阵异样的窸窣，随即陷入片刻的完全寂静。遵照律师昨天郑重其事的叮嘱，艾薇今天完全没有施脂粉。

分分秒秒在紧张中过去，帕克斯顿-史密斯对他这位美丽的当事人满怀叹服。

关于在证人席上应有的举止，他叮嘱过的话，艾薇竟一句不落全记得，包括一些她纵然忘了也情有可原的细节。其中之一便是：回答控方出庭律师的问话时，她该把头高高抬起。

===Original===

This she obviously tried to do, and when, as more than once happened, she threw what looked like a childlike glance of fear and supplication at the kind, if grave, face of the inquisitor whose only desire was to learn the truth and nothing but the truth, a thrill of sympathy went through her great, silent audience.

Again, when the flawless oval of her face appeared framed in the tiny little pull-on black hat, and her starlike eyes for a moment looked wild, many a man, watching her, told himself that he could well understand, indeed almost sympathise with, any crime being committed by one who loved her, and who longed, as only lovers long, to have the exquisite creature standing there at bay entirely his own.

But one curious thing was observed by those who note such things. This was that not once did Ivy Lexton glance at the prisoner in the dock, during the long examination-in-chief.

As for Gretorex, he on his side crossed his arms and stared straight before him as if with unseeing eyes, during the whole of the time the woman he had loved with so devoted and trusting a love, remained in the witness-box.

===Chinese===

这一点她显然尽力做到了。而每当她——这样的时候不止一次——朝那位讯问者投去近乎孩子气的、又惧又求的一瞥时，满庭屏息的听众里便掠过一阵同情的悸动。那位讯问者面容和善而不失庄重，一心只想查明真相，除真相之外别无他求。

再者，当她那张无可挑剔的鹅蛋脸衬在那顶小小的套头黑帽之下，那双星子般的眼睛一时显出惊惶无措时，多少注视着她的男人都暗自思忖：凡是爱她的人——凡是怀着唯有情人才有的那种渴望、想把立在那儿如困兽般的妙人儿独占为私有的一切——无论为她犯下什么罪，他们都能充分理解，甚至几乎要为之动容。

但留意此等事情的人注意到了一桩怪事：在漫长的主讯问（examination-in-chief）过程中，艾薇·莱克斯顿一次也没有朝被告席上的囚犯望过一眼。

至于格雷托雷克斯，他那一方呢，在那位他曾爱得如此深情、如此信重的女子待在证人席里的全部时间里，始终抱着双臂，直勾勾地望着前方，仿佛双目失明一般。

===Original===

Sir Jonathan Wright, the leading counsel for the Crown, to whom fate had assigned Ivy Lexton as his principal witness, was very gentle with her, moved, no doubt, by her evident, shrinking fear. But all those present in Court were struck by the clear way she answered the questions concerning her early married life, the loss of Jervis’s fortune, and his final, successful, effort, to find work.

It was not until she was questioned as to her relations﻿—her “friendship,” as counsel put it﻿—with the prisoner that Ivy’s voice for the first time became inaudible, and that the Judge had to admonish her to speak louder.

But at once she responded with pathetic submission to the flick of the whip.

“I am sorry to have to press the question, Mrs. Lexton”﻿—and the distinguished man on whom had fallen the, to him, painful duty of conducting the prosecution did feel really sorry for this lovely, pathetic-looking, young creature﻿—“but I put to you, and most solemnly, the question: What were your real relations to and with Dr. Gretorex?”

===Chinese===

命运把艾薇·莱克斯顿派作控方首席出庭律师乔纳森·赖特爵士（Sir Jonathan Wright）的主证人。他待她非常温和，无疑是被她那明白可见的瑟缩惧意打动了。但在座众人都为她答话的清楚利落暗暗称奇——无论是答她早年的婚后生活、杰维斯家产的荡尽，还是他最后总算谋到一份差事的经过。

直到被问到她与被告的关系——用律师的话说，她的「交情」——时，艾薇的声音才第一次低得听不见了，法官不得不提醒她说大声些。

但鞭梢轻轻一挥，她便立刻应声，顺从得叫人可怜。

「很抱歉，我不得不追问这个问题，莱克斯顿太太。」——这位身负主持控方重任、深以为苦的名人，对眼前这个楚楚可怜的年轻尤物也确实由衷地觉得不忍——「但我还是要向你、且是最郑重地向你提出这个问题：你与格雷托雷克斯医生之间，究竟是怎样一种真实的关系？」

===Original===

“We were great friends. All three of us were great friends. My husband, too,” she answered, in a tone which, if clear, yet quivered with pain.

“Were your husband and Dr. Gretorex friends before you ever met Dr. Gretorex?”

Then came a whispered, “I don’t know.”

“Eh, what?”

The Judge, Mr. Justice Mayhew, was old, but none the less keen and clear as regarded his mind, though he was slightly deaf.

===Chinese===

「我们是极要好的朋友。我们三个都是极要好的朋友。我丈夫也是。」她答道，声音虽清楚，却痛楚得发颤。

「在你认识格雷托雷克斯医生之前，你丈夫和他已经是朋友了吗？」

接着是一声耳语般的「我不知道」。

「呃，说什么？」

主审的梅休法官（Mr. Justice Mayhew）年事已高，头脑却依然敏锐清明，只是耳朵有些背。

===Original===

“She does not know, my lord,” said Sir Jonathan emphatically.

Then he turned back to his witness.

“Do you think they were already acquainted?”

“I really don’t know.” And then, perhaps because she saw she was creating a less good impression than had been the case with regard to her other answers, “I think not,” she said firmly.

===Chinese===

「她不知道，法官大人。」乔纳森爵士一字一顿地说。

说罢，他回过身来，面对他的证人。

「那你觉得，他们当时已经相识了吗？」

「我实在不知道。」接着，也许是因为她察觉这个回答给人留下的印象不如先前那些好，她又坚决地补上一句：「我想并没有。」

===Original===

As the examination went on, it became clear that Ivy Lexton was painfully anxious to say all that was good of Roger Gretorex. More than once she managed to bring into one of her answers to a short question the fact that he had been kind to her﻿—very, very kind.

And those who listened breathlessly to Ivy’s artless story of how the prisoner in the dock had come to love her, were moved by her apparent surprise and gratitude that he should have been “so kind” to her.

With quivering lips, again and again, in no wise checked by the man who was taking her, step by step, through the story of these last few months, she said a good word for the now tragic lover with whom she had been on those terms﻿—peculiar, and yet how usual nowadays﻿—when a beautiful young married woman, while enchanted to take all she can from a man, will yet give nothing in exchange.

And with every word she uttered, with every apparently spontaneous admission, Ivy threw a secret thought over the sea to Miles Rushworth, and of what he would think tomorrow of what she said, and left unsaid, today.

===Chinese===

讯问继续下去，渐渐可以看清：艾薇·莱克斯顿一心急切得近乎痛苦地，想把罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的好话都说尽。不止一次，她设法在一个本可三言两语答完的回答里，塞进这么一层意思：他一向待她很好——非常非常好。

人们屏息听着艾薇天真烂漫地讲述被告席上的囚犯如何爱上了她，听到她那份显而易见的惊讶与感激——惊讶感激他竟对她「那么好」——无不为之所动。

那人一步步领着她回顾这几个月的始末，却毫不拦阻；她便嘴唇颤抖着，一遍又一遍地为这个如今落得悲剧下场的情人说好话。她与他的那种关系——何等奇特，可如今又是何等寻常！——一个美丽年轻的已婚妇人，欢欢喜喜地从一个男人那里攫取她能攫取的一切，却不肯拿出任何东西作为回报。

而她每吐一个字，每作一次看似脱口而出的招认，都暗暗向海外递去一念，飞向迈尔斯·拉什沃思：明天他读了她今天说的这些话、和她没说的那些话，会作何想法。

===Original===

How strangely drawn out appeared that first portion of her ordeal, to Ivy herself, and to the man now on trial for his life! Not so to those who listened, with ever-increasing curiosity and excitement, to her admissions, omissions, and equivocations.

But it was generally agreed that, as a matter of fact, the murdered man’s wife had very little to reveal, after all. Even the most mindless and stupid of those present knew that the jury only had to look at her, standing there in the witness-box, and then to look at the prisoner in the dock, to know what must have been that young man’s motive for the crime of which he stood accused.

Ivy was so helpless-looking, so fragile, so appealing, as well as so exceedingly lovely. She seemed, indeed, to some of those watching her, like some poor little delicate furry creature caught in a cruel steel trap.

===Chinese===

这场磨难的第一个阶段，在艾薇自己、在那个正为性命受审的男人看来，显得何其古怪地冗长难挨！可是在侧耳听她招认些什么、隐瞒些什么、含糊其词些什么的人们听来，好奇心与兴奋劲儿却愈听愈涨。

但人们普遍承认，事实上，被谋杀者的妻子到底没有什么可抖搂的。在场的人里，连最没头脑、最蠢笨的也知道：陪审团只消看看站在证人席里的她，再看看被告席上的囚犯，便知道这个青年被控罪行的动机必是什么了。

艾薇看上去那样孤立无援，那样柔弱，那样惹人怜惜，又那样美艳绝伦。在注视她的某些人眼里，她真像一只可怜的、毛茸茸的娇嫩小兽，被夹在无情的钢夹子里。

===Original===

Had she flirted dangerously, heartlessly, with Roger Gretorex? Even that seemed doubtful to some of those listening to her low-toned replies to counsel for the Crown.

There were women now watching her intently who had come into Court that day with the strongest prejudice against Ivy Lexton. Yet they were conquered by what appeared to be her effortless, youthful charm, as also by her evident suffering. And then her many pitiful little efforts to say the best she could say for this man who had loved her moved her own sex, in some cases, to tears.

Many a woman there told herself that the witness now in the box had once loved the prisoner in the dock, even though she had not known it then, and though she would deny it, no doubt, even to herself, now.

===Chinese===

她可曾危险地、无情地与罗杰·格雷托雷克斯调情卖俏？听着她低声回答控方出庭律师的问话，有些人连这一点也拿不准了。

有些女人那天走进法庭时，本对艾薇·莱克斯顿怀着极强的成见，此刻也目不转睛地望着她。但她们被她那仿佛毫不费力的青春魅力征服了，也被她明显的痛苦征服了。而她为了替这个爱过她的男人多说好话所做的一次次楚楚可怜的小小努力，更让一些同性听得眼眶发热，落下泪来。

在场的许多女人都在心里对自己说：证人席上这个女子，曾经爱过被告席上的囚犯——虽然她当年并不自知，虽然如今，毫无疑问，她连对自己也一定会矢口否认。

---

===Original===

At last came the moment which Ivy had visualised hundreds of times in the last few days﻿—the moment, that is, when Sir Joseph Molloy rose to begin his cross-examination of the chief witness for the Crown.

The silence that there had been before was as a loud noise to the silence there was now. But, as always happens, three or four people coughed nervously, and were angrily hushed by those about them.

What was Sir Joseph going to do? One thing certainly. It would be nothing less than his bare duty to try to prove to the jury that, because she had taken everything, and given nothing, the beautiful wife of Jervis Lexton had goaded this young man, Roger Gretorex, to the frenzy which leads to crime.

Not long ago Sir Joseph had caused two juries to disagree over what, to the plain man, had been a clear case of murder. That simply because he had been able to prove that the prisoner in the dock, who was a far from prepossessing type of bucolic lover, had been rendered jealous to madness by the foolish girl whom he had killed, and this though his act had been clearly premeditated. That had been a very different case from the Lexton case, and one not nearly so exciting to those in Court. Still, there were many present today who remembered the terrible cross-examination of the poor dead girl’s mother. It had been the way Sir Joseph had dealt with the trembling woman, the admissions he had forced out of her, which had saved his client’s life.

===Chinese===

艾薇在这几天里想象过千百遍的时刻，终于到来了——那就是约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士站起身来，开始对控方头号证人进行交叉盘问的时刻。

方才的那阵寂静，同此刻的寂静一比，简直算得上一片喧哗。但正如每逢此类时刻总免不了的那样，还是有三四个人紧张地咳嗽起来，被周围的人怒声制止。

约瑟夫爵士要做什么？有一点是确定无疑的：他少不得要尽他的本分，向陪审团证明——只因她索取了一切、却不曾付出分毫，杰维斯·莱克斯顿那位美丽的妻子，才把这个青年罗杰·格雷托雷克斯激到了那步引向犯罪的狂乱田地。

就在不久以前，约瑟夫爵士还曾让两届陪审团在一桩明眼人看来铁证如山的谋杀案上争执不下、无法定谳。缘由无他：他证明了被告席上那个远谈不上讨喜的乡下情种，是被他杀死的那个蠢姑娘妒火烧疯了心智——纵然他的行为分明是预谋已久。那桩案子与莱克斯顿案大不相同，对到庭的人也不及本案刺激。尽管如此，今天在场的仍有许多人记得他对那可怜死去的姑娘的母亲那场骇人的盘问。正是约瑟夫爵士对付那个瑟瑟发抖的女人的手段，正是他逼她招出的一句句供词，救了他当事人的性命。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===

But even in a court of law, where everything, in spite of what the more ignorant section of the public may think, is arranged and prearranged, the unexpected sometimes does happen.

To the amazement of everybody present, Sir Joseph Molloy was almost as kindly, as courteous, as careful of hurting her feelings, in his cross-examination of Ivy Lexton, as his good friend Sir Jonathan Wright had been during the examination-in-chief. Indeed Sir Joseph, as a famous descriptive writer declared the next morning, was positively dove-like in his gentleness. When cross-examining Mrs. Jervis Lexton, his object seemed to be simply that of proving that Roger Gretorex, in everyday life, had been a considerate, chivalrous, and extremely unselfish friend. And that had already been freely admitted by the leading counsel for the Crown.

True, there came a moment when Sir Joseph, who found it painfully difficult to play the role he had faithfully promised Roger Gretorex to play, pressed Ivy just a little hard as to what form of words the prisoner had used on the last occasion he had made love to her.

The witness broke down, for the first time, over that probing question, and it was sobbing that she asked: “Must I answer that?”

===Chinese===

然而，即便在法庭上——尽管较为无知的那部分公众另有想法，那里的一切其实都是安排了又安排、预定好了的——意外有时也确实会发生。

令在场众人错愕的是，约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士在对艾薇·莱克斯顿的交叉盘问中，竟几乎同他的好友乔纳森·赖特爵士在主讯问时一样和气、一样彬彬有礼、一样小心着意不去伤她的感情。的确，正如一位著名的纪实作家次日清晨所宣称的，约瑟夫爵士那副温文，简直鸽子一般。他盘问杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太时，目的似乎只是要证明：罗杰·格雷托雷克斯在日常生活中，素来是个体贴入微、侠骨柔肠、极其无私的朋友。而这一点，控方首席出庭律师本已慨然承认过了。

诚然，也有那么一刻，约瑟夫爵士——他发觉，要扮演自己向罗杰·格雷托雷克斯郑重允诺要扮演的那个角色，实在难乎其难——在追问被告最后一次向她求爱时用的究竟是何字眼上，把艾薇逼得略紧了些。

这位证人被这道刨根问底的追问逼得头一回失声痛哭，呜咽着问道：「我必须回答吗？」

===Original===

The Judge explained to her, kindly enough, “Yes, prisoner’s counsel is entitled to an answer to that question. But if you cannot remember the exact form of words which were used on that occasion, you are entitled to say so.”

And then she replied, uttering the words very clearly this time: “I can only remember that he said he loved me, and that were I free he hoped I would become his wife.”

Now those were the last words Sir Joseph had intended Ivy Lexton to utter in answer to his question. And the knowledge that this was so caused a murmur of﻿—was it amusement?﻿—to run through the Court. The public much enjoy hearing a witness score off counsel.

“And what did you answer to that?” he asked sharply.

And then, as Ivy again began sobbing, shrugging his great shoulders he signified that his cross-examination of the principal witness for the Crown was over.

===Chinese===

法官倒颇为和蔼地向她解释：「是的，被告的辩护律师有权得到这个问题的回答。但你若记不起当时所说的确切字句，你也有权如实说明。」

随后她作答，这一回字字清晰：「我只记得他说过他爱我，还说倘若我自由了，他希望我能做他的妻子。」

而那，恰恰是约瑟夫爵士最没打算让艾薇·莱克斯顿在回答他时说出口的话。此中情形一经众人会意，一阵低语——是觉得有趣吗？——掠过整座法庭。看旁听的人最爱瞧的，就是证人驳倒律师。

「那么你当时是怎么回答的？」他厉声问。

随后，见艾薇又开始抽噎，他耸了耸宽肩，示意自己对控方头号证人的交叉盘问就此结束。

===Original===

Some of those present in Court were cruel enough to regret that Sir Joseph had not been up to his usual form. What would not such people have given to have heard what had passed at an interview the great advocate had had, only yesterday, with the man now standing rigid in the dock!

Sir Joseph Molloy always insisted on seeing any man or woman whom he was about to defend on a charge of murder and, at his final interview with Gretorex, the prisoner had begun by begging him most earnestly to refrain from cross-examining Ivy Lexton.

But as to that, his counsel had refused to be guided by the accused man’s wishes.

“Do you expect me to put the hangman’s rope round your neck?” he had asked harshly.

===Chinese===

庭上有些人刻薄得很，竟惋惜约瑟夫爵士不曾拿出他惯常的身手。这位大辩护律师昨天与那个此刻直挺挺立在被告席上的人有过一番晤谈——若能听见他们谈了些什么，这些人什么不肯付出啊！

约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士一贯坚持，凡因杀人罪名延请他辩护的人，无论男女，他必先亲自面见。在与格雷托雷克斯的最后一次晤谈中，被告开口便恳切央求他，千万莫去盘问艾薇·莱克斯顿。

但在这一层上，他的辩护律师拒绝听凭被告的意愿行事。

「您莫非要我亲手把刽子手的绞索套上您的脖颈？」他当时冷硬地质问。

===Original===

Even so, he had promised that he would be very gentle with her. And gentle he had been, all the more gentle, perhaps, because, at the very end of that painful, curious interview, Gretorex had said to him, gazing right into his eyes:

“Treat Mrs. Lexton as you yourself would treat the woman you love, or your own cherished sister, were she in the witness-box and you her cross-examiner.”

So it was that, to his bitter regret, Sir Joseph Molloy had behaved, with regard to Ivy, in a way quite foreign to his nature. What he had longed to do was to turn this lovely little creature inside out, and to apply to her, in his own inimitable, almost affectionately feline, manner, that awful Third Degree before which even the innocent trembled.

After a great deal of anxious thought, he had made up his mind to plead, on his client’s behalf, a fit of temporary insanity. He hoped, that is, to persuade the jury that, by some extraordinary combination of circumstances, Jervis Lexton had been suffering in very truth from some commonplace digestive disorder up to that last day when Gretorex, driven mad by jealousy and love, had done the awful deed. Not, that is, as the slow poisoner goes to work, but as a man takes out a knife to stab his rival to the heart.

That had been, roughly speaking, the line Sir Joseph had intended to take before he had seen his client. But to do that he would have had to play with Ivy Lexton as a powerful cat plays with a young mouse, and that course of action had been absolutely forbidden by the man whom he now believed innocent, even though reason whispered that he must be guilty.

===Chinese===

即便如此，他也答应过待她会非常温和。他也确实温和了——或许还加倍温和，因为在那场痛苦而蹊跷的晤谈收尾时，格雷托雷克斯直直望进他的眼睛，对他说：

「请您把莱克斯顿太太，当作您自己心爱的女人、或您疼爱的亲姐妹来对待——倘若站在证人席里的是她，而盘问她的是您。」

就这样，令他痛悔莫名的是，约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士对艾薇的做派，竟全然有悖他的天性。他本来巴不得把这个可爱的小东西里里外外翻个底朝天，以他自己那套独步无双、近乎温存却又猫戏猎物般的手法，对她施以那连无辜之人也要战栗的、可怕的第三级审讯。

经过再三焦思苦虑，他打定了主意：替当事人申辩一时性精神失常。也就是说，他指望说服陪审团相信：由于某种异乎寻常的机缘凑合，杰维斯·莱克斯顿直到最后那天之前，千真万确一直患着某种平平常常的消化病症；而就在那一天，格雷托雷克斯被妒意与爱火逼得发了狂，才做下那桩可怕的事。也就是说，不是像慢性投毒者那样下手，而是像一个人拔出刀来、直刺情敌心口那样。

大致说来，这本是约瑟夫爵士在见到当事人之前打算走的路数。可要走这条路，他就得像一只壮猫戏弄小老鼠那般摆弄艾薇·莱克斯顿，而这套路数，已被那个他如今信其清白的人断然禁止——纵然理智在他耳畔低语：此人必定有罪。

---

===Original===

Then was it all over﻿—the ordeal she had so dreaded at an end? Ivy felt suddenly as if everything were whirling round her, and indeed she nearly fainted. But she made an effort to pull herself together, and to those who saw her leave the witness-box the expression on her white face appeared deeply pathetic.

“Bravo! You did splendidly! I’m proud of you,” whispered Paxton-Smith.

And then he asked, “Would you like to stay on in Court, or shall I take you home?”

He hoped she would say “Home.”

For a moment she looked undecided, then, “I think I would like to stay,” she murmured.

===Chinese===

那么，这一切都完了么——她那样惧怕的一场酷刑就此了结了？艾薇霎时只觉万物都绕着她打旋，当真险些晕倒。可她奋力定了定神；在看她步出证人席的人们眼里，她那张白脸面上的神情，凄婉已极。

「好极了！你答得漂亮极了！我为你骄傲。」帕克斯顿-史密斯低声道。

接着他问：「你想留在庭上，还是我送你回家？」

他盼着她说「回家」。

她一时显得踌躇，随后喃喃道：「我想我还是留下吧。」

===Original===

Now that her part was done, and with her legal adviser’s “Bravo!” pounding in her ears, she felt she would prefer to be here rather than alone in the flat. Indeed, when Paxton-Smith had found her a comfortable seat among her friends, she began to feel a curious sense of detachment, as if the drama of the trial being played out before her scarcely concerned her personally at all.

This strange feeling was really the measure of her profound relief at having, so to speak, weathered the storm of what everyone had told her would be such an awful experience. Why, it hadn’t been really terrible at all! There had been moments of her examination﻿—not of her cross-examination﻿—which in a sense Ivy had almost enjoyed. She had been able to make herself out, to the great company of people about her, what she believed herself to be﻿—a sweet-natured, unselfish little woman, whom everybody loved.﻿ ﻿…

The appearance of Dr. Berwick in the witness-box gave her a momentary sensation of unease. He was clear, unemotional, and not in the least nervous.

There was a little tiff between him and counsel when he told the story of how he had come in one day, and found the prisoner prescribing for his, Dr. Berwick’s, patient. It was put to him as a fact that Gretorex had not made out any form of prescription and that when he was “caught” apparently doing so, he had only been writing down the name of an ordinary gargle, to be found made up in every chemist’s shop. Also, at the time he had written down the name of that proprietary preparation, Gretorex was under the impression that, Dr. Lancaster having had an accident, the sick man had no one attending him, at any rate at that moment.

===Chinese===

如今她的戏份已经演完，法律顾问那一声「好极了！」还在她耳畔轰响，她觉得自己宁愿在这里待着，也不愿独自回到那套公寓。果然，等帕克斯顿-史密斯在朋友们中间替她觅得一个舒适的座位，她便开始生出一种古怪的抽离之感，仿佛眼前搬演的这出庭审大戏，同她本人全不相干似的。

这种奇怪的感觉，其实正是她如释重负之深的量度：人人都说那将是何等可怕的煎熬，而她，可以说，竟闯过了这场风暴。嗐，根本就算不上可怕！在她的受讯之中——不是交叉盘问——有那么几个片刻，艾薇几乎可说是受用的。她得以当着四周这一大群人，把自己装扮成她自以为是的那个人——一个性情温柔、无私忘我、人人疼爱的小女人……

伯威克医生出现在证人席里，令她一时感到不安。他答得条理分明，不动声色，没有半分紧张。

当他讲到自己某日进门、撞见被告正在给他的病人——他伯威克医生的病人——开方的经过时，他与辩护律师之间起了一点小小的口角。辩护方向他摆出一项事实：格雷托雷克斯并未开出任何形式的处方；而当他在表面上开药时被「撞破」，他写下的不过是一种普通含漱剂的名字，随便哪家药铺都有配好的。再者，写下那味专利成药名字的时候，格雷托雷克斯还以为兰开斯特医生遭了意外，病人已无人诊治——至少那一刻是如此。

===Original===

Even so, Dr. Berwick’s evidence was regarded by all those present as very damaging to the prisoner, and Sir Joseph could do nothing with him, save to denounce him angrily for not having insisted at once, as soon as he became uneasy, on a second opinion.

And then Roger Gretorex’s famous counsel flung by far the greatest sensation of the trial on the Court.

In a quiet, toneless voice he observed, as if he was stating the most natural thing in the world, “I do not call any evidence.”

There ran an excited murmur through the, till now, still audience. Everyone had fully expected that the prisoner would give evidence on his own behalf and, to the great majority of those there, the fact that Gretorex had refused to go into the witness-box, not only signed his own death-warrant, but proved conclusively that Sir Joseph regarded his client as guilty.

===Chinese===

尽管如此，伯威克医生的证词在在场众人看来对被告极为不利，约瑟夫爵士拿他毫无办法，只能怒声斥责他：为何不在刚起疑心时便当即坚持另请会诊。

接着，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯那位声名显赫的辩护律师，把整场庭审中最强烈的一次轰动掷到了法庭上。

他以一种平静而无抑扬的嗓音，仿佛在陈述天下再自然不过的事情，说道：「我方不传召任何证据。」

一阵激动的低语掠过此前一直静默的旁听席。人人都料定被告会亲自出庭作证；而在场的大多数人看来，格雷托雷克斯拒绝进入证人席这一事实，非但等于亲手签下自己的死刑执行令，而且明白无误地证明：约瑟夫爵士认定他的当事人有罪。

===Original===

At first Ivy did not understand what it was that had happened. Then two or three of the friends by whom she was surrounded excitedly explained the exact meaning of Sir Joseph’s apparently casual remark.

She, the woman in the case, said nothing in answer to these eager, wordy explanations. Indeed, she seemed hardly to take in all that was being told her. But inwardly she was feeling, oh! so thankful, so intensely thankful, that Roger Gretorex had refused to give evidence. She had been so horribly afraid that when in the witness-box her onetime lover might, unwittingly, give her away.

It was of Miles Rushworth that she was thinking, ever thinking, deep in what she called her heart. He had been to her the only audience that mattered, while she had been examined and cross-examined as to her relations with the prisoner.

“And now,” whispered someone just behind her, “comes the closing speech for the Crown.”

Ivy was not particularly interested in the closing speech for the Crown. And, truth to tell, neither was anyone else in Court, excepting, it must be hoped, the jury.

===Chinese===

起初，艾薇不明白出了什么事。随后，围在她身边的两三位朋友激动地解释起约瑟夫爵士那句看似随口的话的确切含义。

她，这桩案子的女主角，面对这些争先恐后、絮絮叨叨的解说，一言不发。的确，人们对她说的话，她似乎并没有听进去多少。可她心底正觉着——啊！多么感激，多么铭感不尽——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯拒绝了出庭作证。她先前怕得要死，生怕昔日情郎一旦站上证人席，会不知不觉把她供出来。

在她自称的那颗心的深处，她想着的是迈尔斯·拉什沃思，念念不忘的正是他。当她被讯问、被盘问她与被告的关系时，满庭听众当中，唯有他一个，才是她真正在意的看客。

「现在，」她背后有人低声道，「该控方作终结陈词了。」

控方的终结陈词，艾薇并不怎么感兴趣。说实话，庭上旁的人也没谁感兴趣——除了陪审团诸位，但愿如此。

===Original===

Sir Jonathan sat down at the end of﻿—was it twenty minutes, or only a quarter of an hour?

There was a short moment of breathing space, and then all prepared to give their best attention while Sir Joseph Molloy started what was afterwards described as “his great speech for the defence.”

But though it may have been a great speech, it was a very short speech, for the famous advocate knew that the only hope of saving Gretorex from the gallows would be to make the kind of appeal to the jury which is always made in France with regard to what is called there a *crime passionnel*.

The fact that Sir Joseph had not elected to call any evidence gave him, as all those instructed in the law who listened to him were well aware, the last word. And he made the most of his privilege.

More than once during the course of what was simply a noble panegyric of Roger Gretorex, the prisoner went from deathly pale to very red. He would have given many of his few remaining days of life to close his powerful advocate’s mouth. Also, what was the use of it all? Gretorex knew as well as did the Judge that the fact that he was high-minded, chivalrous, the best of sons to a widowed mother, and a man whose money affairs were in perfect order, had nothing to do with the question as to whether he had committed the murder for which he was being tried. Neither, for the matter of that, would the fact that he had adored the wife of Jervis Lexton, and had gone temporarily mad for love of her, save him from the gallows.

===Chinese===

乔纳森爵士讲完落座——用了二十分钟呢，还是只一刻钟？

短短片刻的喘息之后，众人纷纷备好全副注意力，听约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士开始那篇日后被称做「他伟大的辩护词」的演说。

不过，这篇演说纵使堪称伟大，却也短得很，因为这位大名鼎鼎的辩护律师心里明白：要救格雷托雷克斯脱离绞刑架，唯一的指望，就是向陪审团发出在法国总要针对所谓「激情犯罪」（crime passionnel）发出的那一类恳求。

约瑟夫爵士未传召任何证据这一事实——凡在场谙熟法律的人都心知肚明——把最后陈词的权利留给了他。这份特权，他也用到了十足。

这篇通篇不过是对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的一篇高华颂词的演说，讲到中途，被告不止一回由死灰般的苍白转为通红。哪怕要他舍出自己所剩无几的寿数里的许多天，他也甘愿换得他那雄辩的辩护人就此住口。再说，这一切又有何用？格雷托雷克斯同法官一样明白：他心地高尚、为人侠义、是寡母最好的儿子、银钱事务打理得一丝不苟，这些同他究竟有没有犯下受审的这桩谋杀，毫不相干。同样，他深爱杰维斯·莱克斯顿的妻子、为她一时神智癫狂，这事实也救不得他脱离绞刑架。

===Original===

One man, on whom by accident the prisoner’s eyes were fixed for a moment, actually shrugged his shoulders, when Sir Joseph brought in a swift allusion to the fact that one of the prisoner’s great-uncles had died in a lunatic asylum.

Now the Judge desired﻿—indeed they all desired﻿—to finish the case that day.

“Do you wish to stay on for the summing-up and for the verdict?” whispered Paxton-Smith to his client.

Again he hoped that Ivy would﻿—well? have the decency to rise and say, “No, I will go home, now.”

But instead of saying “No,” she whispered, “Yes, I think I should like to do that.”

===Chinese===

当约瑟夫爵士飞快地捎带一笔，提到被告的一位叔祖死在疯人院里这桩事实时，有个恰好被被告目光一时凝住的人，竟当真耸了耸肩。

此刻，法官希望——说实在的，庭上人人都希望——当天就把本案了结。

「你愿意留下听完总结陈词（summing-up）和裁决（verdict）吗？」帕克斯顿-史密斯低声问他的当事人。

他又一次盼着艾薇能——怎么说呢？——识趣地起身说：「不，我现在就回家。」

可她没有说「不」，只低声道：「好的，我想我愿意留下。」

===Original===

And then, in slow, impressive tones, the Judge began his summing-up.

Though Mr. Justice Mayhew took what seemed to be a considerable time, his was one of the shortest addresses to a jury ever delivered in an important trial for murder at the Old Bailey. The story he had to recapitulate was, in a sense, so very ordinary. It had been unfolded, in all its stark simplicity, by a tiny handful of witnesses, including of course the most important of them all, the young wife of the murdered man.

Even so, speaking himself with impressive clarity, the Judge went over the now well-known tale step by step. And finally his lordship directed the jury that the fact that a man has been what is loosely called “driven mad” by love does not mean that he is not capable of keeping command over his faculties. Why, at the very time this was supposed to have happened to the prisoner, he was in charge of a large medical practice, and carrying out all the responsible, anxious duties attached to such a practice in an admirable manner!

“We have here the not uncommon case of a strong man’s infatuation for a beautiful woman who gives him little, if any, encouragement,” he observed.

And there was a murmur of disapproval when one of Ivy’s women friends gave a sudden little cackle of laughter, to the shocked surprise of everyone in Court.

===Chinese===

随后，法官以缓慢而庄重的语调，开始作总结陈词。

梅休法官虽然看似费了相当工夫，他这篇对陪审团的陈词，已是老贝利重大谋杀审判中向陪审团所作的最简短的几篇之一。他须复述的这段故事，从某种意义上说，实在平常得很。它已由寥寥几名证人——当然包括其中最重要的那位、被害人的年轻妻子——以其赤裸裸的简单面目，铺陈得清清楚楚。

即便如此，法官仍以令人心折的明晰，把这段如今已尽人皆知的故事一步一步重述过去。最后，法官大人指示陪审团：一个人被所谓爱情「逼疯」这一事实，并不等于他就无力统驭自己的心智。嗐，就在据信此事落到被告身上的那段时日里，他正掌管着一桩规模可观的诊务，并以令人赞叹的方式，履行着这般诊务所系的种种责任重大、忧劳交加的职守！

「我们眼前这桩案子，无非是一个硬朗汉子迷恋上一位美人，而这位美人纵非全然、也几乎不曾给他半点鼓励——此等情形并不罕见。」他说道。

这时，艾薇的一位女友突然发出一声短促的咯咯轻笑，满座为之愕然，四下里顿时响起一片不以为然的低哼。

===Original===

The Judge brushed aside with relentless logic any effect that might have been produced on the jury by Sir Joseph Molloy’s moving account of Gretorex as a wise, unselfish physician, a devoted son, and within the possible limit a generous landlord. No doubt all that was true. But the whole history of crime was there to prove that a person could be all these excellent things, while being also a cruel, callous, secret murderer.

In this case, assuming Gretorex was guilty, the man who had been slowly done to death had been the secret poisoner’s own familiar friend, his boon-companion in many a party of careless pleasure. Further, the man before them, the prisoner in the dock, had not even dared to go into the witness-box. The Judge pointed out in solemn, measured tones that he had the right to comment, and comment most seriously, on that omission.

As the afternoon wore itself away, everyone became very weary. Even Ivy began to wish that she had gone away when Paxton-Smith had last suggested that she should do so.

She stole a glance at Roger Gretorex. He was looking straight at the Judge, with a thoughtful, measuring glance. He looked far more himself, his reserved, intelligent self, than he had done when Sir Joseph was engaged in making that dramatic, useless appeal to the jury.

Ivy Lexton gazed at the prisoner in the dock with a strange feeling at her heart. In a sense she was still proud of this man who had been her devout, adoring lover. He looked so brave, so cool, so completely self-possessed. The majority of those who now and again glanced his way to see how he was “taking it” thought him revoltingly callous.

===Chinese===

约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士动情地把格雷托雷克斯描绘成一位睿智无私的医生、一个至孝的儿子、一个在力所能及的限度内颇为慷慨的房东；法官却以无情的逻辑，把这些话可能在陪审团心头激起的任何效应一扫而空。这一切无疑属实。可是整个犯罪史都在那里作证：一个人尽可以是这许多美德的化身，同时又是一个残忍、冷酷、暗中下手的杀人犯。

在本案中，姑且假定格雷托雷克斯有罪，那么那个被慢慢毒杀至死的人，正是这位暗中投毒者自己的至交密友，是他多场纵情行乐的宴会上的酒伴。再者，他们眼前的这个人、被告席上的这个囚犯，竟连踏进证人席都不敢。法官以庄严而顿挫有度的语调指出：他有权就这一缺漏置评，而且是最严重地置评。

午后光阴渐渐消磨过去，人人都疲惫不堪。连艾薇也懊悔起来，方才帕克斯顿-史密斯劝她走时，自己竟没有走。

她偷眼看了看罗杰·格雷托雷克斯。他正直视着法官，目光沉吟而有掂量。比起约瑟夫爵士向陪审团发出那番悲壮而无用的呼吁时，他此刻的样子更像他自己了——那个矜持、聪慧的自己。

艾薇·莱克斯顿凝望着被告席上的囚犯，心头涌起一种奇异的感觉。在某种意义上，她仍为这个男人骄傲——他曾是她的虔敬而痴心的情人。他看上去那么无畏，那么镇定，那么全然的自持。那些不时瞟他一眼、看他如何「消受」这场审判的人，多半觉得他麻木得叫人憎恶。

===Original===

And, at the same moment that Ivy was doing so, Roger’s mother stole a look at him. Her heart was full of such agony that she felt as if merciful death might suddenly intervene and end it all for her. And her agony was shared, one is tempted to say, almost to the full, by the girl who sat beside her.

At last the summing up was over. The prisoner was taken below, and then began the waiting for the verdict.

To many of those in Court the jury seemed to be away a long, long time. Yet as a matter of fact, it was only a bare half-hour before all those who had gone out to stretch their legs, and talk over for the hundredth time the only real point of mystery in the story, came swarming back into the Court.

Slowly, almost in a leisurely way, the nine jurymen and the three jurywomen filed in. Some of those present noticed that not a single juror looked at the prisoner, who had been brought back to the dock and now stood at ease between two warders.

Although the verdict was a foregone conclusion, every human being there looked strained, anxious; and Ivy Lexton again felt sick and faint. For the first time since she stepped into the witness-box no one was looking at her, for everyone was looking at the jury.

===Chinese===

而就在艾薇这样望着他的同一刻，罗杰的母亲也在偷眼看他。她心中盛满那样的剧痛，只觉仿佛仁慈的死会突然降临，替她把这一切做个了断。而坐在她身旁的那个姑娘——人们几乎要说——也全份分担着她的痛楚。

总结陈词终于完毕。被告被押了下去，接着便开始了等待裁决的时辰。

在庭上许多人看来，陪审团似乎出去了许久许久。可事实上，不过将将半个钟头，那些出去伸伸腿脚、把案情中唯一真正的疑点第一百次议论一遍的人，便都涌回了法庭。

九位男陪审员与三位女陪审员缓缓鱼贯而入，近乎从容不迫。在场有些人留意到，没有一个陪审员看向被告——被告已被押回被告席，此刻随意地站在两名看守中间。

尽管裁决早已是意料中事，庭上每一张脸都紧绷而焦灼；艾薇·莱克斯顿又觉着恶心眩晕。自她踏进证人席以来，头一回没有一个人看她，因为人人都在看着陪审团。

===Original===

Everything being now ready, the Judge returned to the bench, all those who were seated in Court rising to their feet.

The Judge, having bowed to the Court, seated himself, and so, apparently, did everyone else there, except the three men, the prisoner and his custodians, standing in the dock.

Then the Clerk of the Court asked the fateful question:

“Members of the jury, have you agreed upon your verdict?”

There was a pause, but at last the foreman of the jury, a nervous, intelligent-looking man, who was evidently intensely relieved that his responsible task was now over, answered in a clear tone, “We have.”

===Chinese===

一切就绪，法官回到法官席上，庭内所有坐着的人一齐起立。

法官向法庭一躬，随即落座；庭上其余的人看来也都坐下了，只有被告席里站着的三个人除外——被告和他的两名看守。

接着，法庭书记官（Clerk of the Court）提出了那个定夺生死的问题：

「陪审团的诸位，你们是否已就裁决取得一致？」

静默片刻，陪审团团长终于开口。这是个神情紧张、面相聪慧的人，显然因肩负的重任此刻已了而大松了一口气。他语调清朗地答道：「已经一致。」

===Original===

“Do you find the prisoner, Roger Kingston Gretorex, guilty or not guilty of the wilful murder of Jervis Lexton?”

There was a scarcely perceptible wait, and then came the one word﻿—“Guilty.”

And it was as if there swept a great sigh through the now lighted Court, followed by a sudden buzz of talk.

But this was instantly quelled when the ushers cried sternly, “Silence!”

All eyes were now fixed on the prisoner. He was standing far more stiffly to attention than he had done a moment ago, as the clear tones of the Clerk of the Court rang out:

“Roger Kingston Gretorex, you stand convicted of wilful murder. Have you anything to say for yourself why the Court should not give you judgment according to law?”

===Chinese===

「你们认定被告罗杰·金斯顿·格雷托雷克斯（Roger Kingston Gretorex）蓄意谋杀杰维斯·莱克斯顿，是有罪还是无罪？」

几乎难以察觉的停顿之后，一个词落了下来——「有罪。」

此时法庭里已亮起灯，仿佛有一声浩大的叹息席卷全场，紧接着骤然响起一片嗡嗡的议论。

但庭警厉声喝出「肃静！」，议论当即被压了下去。

所有目光此刻都钉在被告身上。法庭书记官清朗的语声响起时，他立得远比刚才挺直，几如立正：

「罗杰·金斯顿·格雷托雷克斯，你已被判定蓄意谋杀罪名成立。你可有话要说，说明本庭为何不应依法对你下判？」

===Original===

“Only that I am innocent.”

The five words were uttered in a cool, firm tone.

It was the second time during the whole course of the trial that anyone there had heard Roger Gretorex’s voice.

Ivy felt better now, and she watched everything that went on with eager, excited interest.

===Chinese===

「我是无辜的。」

这五个字，以冷静而坚定的语调吐出。

这是整个庭审过程中，庭上的人第二回听到罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的声音。

艾薇此刻觉着好受了些，怀着急切而兴奋的心情，注视着眼前发生的一切。

===Original===

Sitting near the Judge was a young man to whom no one had before paid any special attention. But now every eye was fixed on him, for it was he who lifted a square of black cloth, and placed it, with careful deliberation, on the Judge’s wig.

Then solemn, slow, emphatic tones of admonition fell on the heavy air. They were not cruel words, for the Judge felt deeply sorry for the young man before him. He had heard, only last evening at a dinner-party, something of the quiet, kindly, useful life that Mrs. Gretorex and her son had both led since the death of the husband and father who had caused their financial ruin.

And then came the awful words﻿—

“Roger Kingston Gretorex, the jury, after a careful and patient hearing, have found you guilty of the wilful murder of Jervis Lexton. The sentence of the Court upon you is that you be taken from here to a lawful prison, and from there to a place of execution; and that you be there hanged by the neck until you be dead; and that your body be buried within the precincts of the prison in which you shall have been last confined after your conviction; and may the Lord have mercy on your soul.”

As Ivy Lexton, supported by a number of her friends and acquaintances, left the Old Bailey by a back way, she chanced in the passage to meet Mrs. Gretorex face to face. The eyes of the two women crossed﻿—and a stab of horrible pain flashed across the worn, yet even now calm, face of Roger Gretorex’s mother.

===Chinese===

法官近旁坐着一位年轻人，先前谁也不曾对他多加留意。可此刻每一双眼睛都盯住了他，因为正是他，掀起一方黑布，郑重而从容地安放在法官的假发之上。

随后，庄严、缓慢、一字一顿的训诫之声，沉落在滞重的空气里。那些话并不残酷，因为法官对眼前的年轻人深感痛惜。就在昨夜的一场晚宴上，他还听闻了格雷托雷克斯太太母子二人的一些境况——自那位拖垮他们家产的丈夫和父亲亡故以来，母子俩一直过着这样安静、善良、于人有益的生活。

接着，那可怕的话音落了下来——

「罗杰·金斯顿·格雷托雷克斯，陪审团经过细致而耐心的听审，认定你犯有蓄意谋杀杰维斯·莱克斯顿之罪。本庭对你的判决是：将你带离此处，押往一座合法监狱，再从那里押往刑场；你将在那里被施以绞刑，缢颈至死；你的遗体将于你定罪后最后羁押的监狱界墙之内掩埋。愿上帝怜悯你的灵魂。」

艾薇·莱克斯顿由一群朋友故旧搀扶着，取后路离开老贝利时，在过道里恰与格雷托雷克斯太太迎面相遇。两个女人的目光交会了——一阵可怕的痛楚，闪过罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲那张憔悴却依然平静的脸。

## XVII / 十七

===Original===

“I took Mrs. Gretorex a nice cup of tea at seven o’clock, for I heard her moving about even before then. But the poor lady only just sipped it. She said her throat seemed swollen, so she couldn’t swallow. But she’s up now, and I do wish, miss, you’d go in and try and persuade her to have just a little bit of breakfast. Me and my husband﻿—well﻿—we both fairly broke down and cried last night, when we thought of how we’d feel if it was our boy that was going to be hanged by the neck till he was dead.”

“I hope she doesn’t let her mind dwell on that,” Enid’s pale face went a shade paler, as she looked into the kind, pitying eyes of Mrs. Gretorex’s landlady.

“How can she help hearing those awful words a-ringing in her ears? Why they rings in mine, ever since yesterday! I’m sorry I did stay till the end. A friend warned me, so she did. She says to me, ‘Maria, you’ll enjoy every bit of it up to the jury coming back. But if I was you I’d leave the Court before the Judge puts on his black cap.’ I wish I’d done that now!”

As Enid advanced into the sitting-room, and saw Mrs. Gretorex’s figure leaning back in the deep grandfather’s chair, she thought for one moment﻿—and to her it was a blessed moment﻿—that her dear old friend was dead, that she had died, literally, of a broken heart, so rigid was the lonely looking figure, so calm and white the face, so pale the lips.

But Roger Gretorex’s mother was not dead, and hearing Enid’s footsteps, she opened her eyes.

===Chinese===

「七点钟我给格雷托雷克斯太太端去了一杯热腾腾的好茶，因为还不到七点，我就听见她在屋里走动了。可这位可怜的太太只抿了那么一小口。她说嗓子眼儿像是肿了，咽东西咽不下去。不过她现在已经起来了，小姐，我求求您进去劝劝她，好歹吃一点儿早饭。我跟我们家那口子——唉——昨儿晚上一想到，要是换成咱们的儿子，要被人用绳子缢住脖子吊到死，我们两口子简直都忍不住哭了一场。」

「但愿她别总往那上头想。」伊妮德说。她望着格雷托雷克斯太太那位房东太太一双和善怜惜的眼睛，自己苍白的脸又白了一层。

「那些可怕的话在耳朵眼里嗡嗡响个不停，她怎么躲得开呢？可不是嘛，从昨天起，那些话也在我耳朵眼里直响！我真后悔自己待到了最后。有位朋友事先提醒过我，真的提醒过。她跟我说：『玛利亚，陪审团退席之前，你每一步都看得津津有味。可我要是你，就会趁法官还没戴上那顶黑帽（black cap，宣判死刑时戴的帽子），赶紧离开法庭。』如今我真后悔没听她的！」

伊妮德走进起居室，看见格雷托雷克斯太太的身影仰靠在那把老式高背大扶手椅深处，有一刹那——于她简直是蒙福的一刹那——她以为这位亲爱的老朋友已经死了，以为她真真切切死于心碎：那孤零零的身影多么僵直，那张脸多么安详苍白，那两片嘴唇多么没有血色。

可是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的母亲并没有死。听见伊妮德的脚步声，她睁开了眼睛。

===Original===

“I want you to have something to eat, Mrs. Gretorex. Mr. Oram told me yesterday that he thought you might be allowed to see Roger today. You must keep up your strength.”

“I will,” said the other quietly. “But it’s an odd thing, Enid, when that kind soul brought in my cup of tea this morning I found I couldn’t swallow. Perhaps I can now. At any rate I’ll try.”

Enid came up a little closer to the big chair.

“I wonder if you would think it strange if I went and had a talk with Mr. Finch?” she said a little nervously.

“With Mr. Finch?” Mrs. Gretorex looked surprised.

===Chinese===

「您得吃点东西才行，格雷托雷克斯太太。奥拉姆先生昨天对我说，他觉得今天也许会准您见罗杰。您千万要撑住身子。」

「我会的。」对方平静地说。「只是有桩怪事，伊妮德，今天早晨那位好心的太太把茶端进来，我竟发觉自己咽不下去。也许这会儿能咽了。无论如何，我总要试试。」

伊妮德朝那把大椅子又凑近了些。

「不知您会不会觉得奇怪，要是我去找芬奇先生谈一谈？」她说得有些紧张。

「找芬奇先生谈？」格雷托雷克斯太太面露诧异。

===Original===

“From something he said the other day, I gathered that he has some theory which is not shared by Mr. Oram. I should like to know what it is. Somehow I feel that there must be something we could do﻿—”

There was such a fervour of revolt, of anguish, in the steady voice, that the older woman for one moment forgot her own agony.

She felt deeply moved; even she had not realised how much Enid cared. The girl had kept an entire curb over her feeling during the terrible days that the two had sat next to one another in Court.

She got up from her chair, and came close up to Enid Dent.

“I feel as if there was nothing left, for me at any rate, to do but to endure.”

===Chinese===

「前几天他说话里透出一点口风，我听出来他心里存着一套想法，是奥拉姆先生不赞成的。我想知道那究竟是什么。不知怎么，我总觉得我们一定还能做点什么——」

那平稳的嗓音里，蕴含着那样一股抗争的热烈、那样一股悲怆的痛楚，刹那之间，年长的妇人竟忘却了自己的苦楚。

她深深动了情；连她也不曾意识到，伊妮德的情意有这么深。在两人并肩坐在法庭里的那些可怕的日子里，这姑娘一直把自己的感情勒得严严实实。

她从椅子里起身，走到伊妮德·登特近前。

「我觉得已经没有什么可做的了——至少对我来说没有——除了忍受。」

===Original===

“I feel,” cried the girl, “as if there was a great deal left to do! Mr. Oram is an old man, Mrs. Gretorex, and though he’s been so awfully good to us, I’ve had the feeling﻿—” then she stopped.

“I know,” replied Mrs. Gretorex in a low voice. “I realise, too, that he believes that Roger did that awful thing.”

“But Mr. Finch,” said Enid eagerly, “knows that Roger is innocent! Would you mind my going and seeing him? He’s always at the office long before Mr. Oram arrives there in the morning.”

“You can do exactly what you think best. I trust you entirely; so, I know, does Roger.”

===Chinese===

「我觉得，」姑娘喊道，「还有好多事可做呢！奥拉姆先生上了年纪，格雷托雷克斯太太，虽说他对我们好得没话说，可我心里总有那么一种感觉——」说到这里，她收住了。

「我知道，」格雷托雷克斯太太低声答道。「我也明白，他相信那件可怕的事是罗杰干的。」

「可是芬奇先生，」伊妮德急切地说，「知道罗杰是无辜的！您在意我去见他一面吗？他每天总是老早就到了事务所，比奥拉姆先生早得多。」

「你觉得怎样最好，就怎样做吧。我完全信得过你；我知道，罗杰也信得过你。」

---

===Original===

Twenty minutes later Enid Dent was sitting in the rather drab-looking waiting-room, lined with steel-bound boxes, where she had spent, during the last few weeks, many dreary, anxious minutes. But this time she had not long to wait, for Mr. Oram’s head clerk soon appeared, a look of surprise on his face.

“I’m afraid you’ll think I’ve come very early,” she said nervously. “But the truth is, Mr. Finch, I wanted to have a short talk with you. And I was anxious to see you before Mr. Oram arrived here this morning.”

“You come up now, at once, to Mr. Oram’s room, Miss Dent. He won’t be here today, at all.”

A feeling of relief swept over Enid Dent. She had a regard for Mr. Oram, but she was also rather afraid of him. And she resented keenly a fact which had become at once apparent to her﻿—that he was convinced, even if most unwillingly, of his client’s guilt.

Enid had a direct, honest nature, as have so many people who yet do not wear their hearts upon their sleeves. So it was that when the two were together in Mr. Oram’s room, she began without any preamble:

===Chinese===

二十分钟后，伊妮德·登特已经坐在那间灰扑扑的候客室里——四壁立着一排排包铁皮的箱柜——近几个星期来，她曾在这里熬过许多阴郁焦灼的时刻。不过这一回她没等多久，奥拉姆先生的主任书记员很快就出来了，一脸惊讶。

「恐怕您会觉得我来得太早了，」她不安地说。「可实情是，芬奇先生，我想同您短短谈一谈。我急着要赶在今早奥拉姆先生到这儿之前见到您。」

「您这就上楼去，登特小姐，直接到奥拉姆先生的房间去。他今天压根儿不会来了。」

一阵如释重负之感涌遍伊妮德·登特全身。她对奥拉姆先生心怀敬重，可也有几分怕他。而且有一桩事实，她一眼便看了出来，为此她愤懑难平——他相信自己的当事人有罪，纵然这信念是千不情万不愿才落下的。

伊妮德生性直率诚实——世上许多人都是如此，却又并不把心事挂在脸上。因此，两人到了奥拉姆先生的房间里，她开门见山，连一句铺垫也没有：

===Original===

“From something you said the other day, Mr. Finch, I understood that you believe there’s some as yet quite unsuspected mystery behind this story of Jervis Lexton’s death. If I am right, will you tell me what it is you do think? Also whether anything can be done to bring the truth to light before﻿—” And then once more she repeated that word “before,” which may mean so very much or so very little in life.

Alfred Finch felt and looked uncomfortable. To make a remark in a casual way is very different from being pinned down, and asked what it is exactly that you meant by saying what you did. But the girl was looking at him with such anxious, appealing eyes, and, after all, if he was a fool, he was being a fool in good company, that of the great Sir Joseph Molloy himself.

Mr. Oram’s head clerk and the famous counsel had become friends over the Lexton case, and what Mr. Finch was about to expound to Enid Dent as his own theory was really Sir Joseph Molloy’s theory. Not, as he intended carefully to explain, as to what had certainly happened, but as to what might conceivably have happened, to provide an explanation of what seemed inexplicable﻿—how, that is, Jervis Lexton had come by his death.

“You may be aware,” he began a little nervously, “that there is a section of the public, including whom I may call our most noted amateur criminologists, who are convinced that we needn’t go further than Mrs. Lexton herself. Their view is that, apart from Mr. Roger, no one else, except the lady who is now his widow, can have had the slightest motive for getting rid of poor Lexton. Now I don’t share that view at all!”

“Neither do I,” said Enid quickly. “We all know that Mrs. Lexton had every reason for wanting her husband to remain alive.”

===Chinese===

「芬奇先生，从您前几天说的话里，我听出来您相信，杰维斯·莱克斯顿之死的背后，还藏着一层至今无人疑心的隐情。要是我没领会错，您可愿意告诉我，您心里究竟作何想法？还有，在……之前，还有没有办法叫真相大白——」说到这里，她又把那个词重复了一遍——「之前」。人的一生里，这个词有时意味着那么多，有时又意味着那么少。

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇只觉得浑身不自在，脸上也挂不住。随口说句闲话，与被人钉住了追问、非要你交代清楚那句话究竟作何解释，完全是两回事。可这姑娘正用那样焦灼、那样恳求的眼神望着他；再说了，他纵然是在犯傻，这傻也犯得有伴儿——陪他一道犯的，不是别人，正是大名鼎鼎的约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士本人。

奥拉姆先生的主任书记员与那位赫赫有名的出庭律师，是在莱克斯顿案里结下的交情。芬奇先生就要对伊妮德·登特阐发的这套「他自己的」理论，其实是约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士的理论。而且——这也正是他打算小心说明的——这套理论说的并非确实发生了什么，而是不妨假定可能发生了什么，好替那看似无从解释的事寻出一个解释：也就是说，杰维斯·莱克斯顿究竟是怎么死的。

「您也许有所耳闻，」他开腔时有点紧张，「有一部分公众——其中不乏我们不妨称作最负盛名的业余犯罪学家的人物——坚信不必再往旁人身上找，莱克斯顿太太本人就是。他们的看法是：除了罗杰先生，再没有别人，唯独如今做了他遗孀的那位太太，才可能有一丝一毫的动机，要除掉可怜的莱克斯顿。这种看法，我可是一点儿都不赞成！」

「我也不赞成，」伊妮德应声说。「我们都清楚，莱克斯顿太太有的是理由，巴望她丈夫活下去。」

===Original===

“But what I do believe,” and a queer expression came over Alfred Finch’s shrewd face, “is that Mrs. Lexton knows a good deal more than she admitted yesterday in the witness-box. I can’t help suspecting that there is some man, a stranger to the case, but well known to her, who had some vital interest in compassing Jervis Lexton’s death. Far stranger things happen in real life than any novelist would dare to put in a story, Miss Dent. Men, aye, and women too, are more unscrupulous than any ordinary person would imagine. Even Mr. Oram would agree to that. If we can find among Mrs. Lexton’s admirers﻿—and she has a goodish few﻿—some wealthy man who is in love with her, *and who has easy access to any form of arsenic*, we might have something like new evidence to offer when the case comes up on appeal.”

“But if that is true,” said Enid quickly, “then the nurse must have known that Mr. Lexton had some other visitor within a few hours of his death﻿—and the parlourmaid who gave evidence as to admitting Dr. Gretorex the afternoon before Mr. Lexton died must have known it too.”

Alfred Finch looked straight into the girl’s now flushed, anxious face.

“What is it,” he asked impressively, “that rules the whole world today?”

And, as he saw she looked bewildered, he snapped out the one word, “Money! What Mrs. Lexton and her lot call ‘the ready.’ ”

===Chinese===

「可我所相信的是，」阿尔弗雷德·芬奇那张精明的脸上掠过一丝古怪的神情，「莱克斯顿太太知道的内情，远比她昨天在证人席上招认的多。我禁不住怀疑有这么一个男人——与本案素不相干，却与她相熟——他在谋害杰维斯·莱克斯顿的性命上，有着性命交关的利害。真实生活里发生的事，比任何小说家敢写进书里的都要离奇得多，登特小姐。男人，是啊，还有女人，都比寻常人想象的更不择手段。这一点，连奥拉姆先生也会点头。倘若我们能在莱克斯顿太太的仰慕者当中——她这样的人，仰慕者为数可着实不少——寻出某一个爱着她的阔男人，*而且能轻易弄到任何形态的砒霜*，那么等本案上诉的时候，我们或许就有点像样的新证据可提了。」

「可要是当真如此，」伊妮德马上说，「那护士必定知道，莱克斯顿先生死前几个钟头里还另有来客——还有那位作证说、在莱克斯顿先生死前那个下午放格雷托雷克斯医生进门的客厅女仆（parlourmaid），也必定知道。」

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇直望着姑娘此刻涨红了、满是焦灼的脸。

「是什么东西，」他郑重其事地问，「在主宰着今天的世界？」

见她一脸茫然，他啪地甩出一个字：「钱！就是莱克斯顿太太和她们那号人嘴里的『现钱』。」

===Original===

What did he mean? In what way could money have played a part in this sinister business?

His next words enlightened her.

“Money can do anything nowadays, for everybody wants money, and spends money as they never did before. Believe me, Miss Dent, no one, in these days, is incorruptible.”

“What a terrible thing to say﻿—even to think!” exclaimed Enid.

“It may be terrible, but it’s the truth, Miss Dent! And it’s particularly true when one considers the people who were mixed up in this affair. All women, and poor themselves, mark you. I don’t mean to imply for a moment that they meant to connive at murder. My view is that their mouths, maybe, were shut with gold before they had any idea what it was that they were going to be asked to stay ‘mum’ about. But then? Well, then, they wisely, in their own interest, continued to keep their mouths shut!”

===Chinese===

他这话是什么意思？在这桩阴险的勾当里，钱又能插进哪一只脚？

他接下来的一句话，让她恍然大悟。

「如今这年头，钱什么都能办到，因为人人都要钱，人人花钱也从没有像今天这样阔气过。相信我，登特小姐，这年头没有一个人是收买不动的。」

「这话多可怕——想想都可怕！」伊妮德喊道。

「也许可怕，可这是实情，登特小姐！尤其是把卷进这桩案子的那些人放在心里掂一掂，更是实情。您留意，全是女人，而且个个自己都穷。我绝没有半分意思说她们存心纵容杀人。依我看，她们的嘴，也许早在还不知道人家要她们对什么事『只字不提』之前，就叫金子给堵上了。可后来呢？唉，后来嘛，她们为着自己的利益，也就识相地接着把嘴闭紧！」

===Original===

“I see,” said Enid slowly.

“I was very much struck by the demeanour of that Nurse Bradfield in the witness-box,” he went on eagerly. “Sir Joseph reduced her to a drivelling state of terror. Now, why was that? You remember, maybe, how he pressed her as to who else came to the flat besides Mr. Roger; and how at last she had to admit that several ladies and gentlemen came there, and that often she really didn’t know who they were! Now, isn’t that a singular thing? We have the master of the house lying ill﻿—not ill enough to have a night nurse, but still, ill enough to have a nurse all day. And yet a lot of people come and go﻿—to lunch, to play bridge, and to take Mrs. Lexton out in the evening! Now that, to me, sounds very odd, not to say suspicious.”

“I quite agree that it does seem strange and heartless,” said Enid in a troubled tone. “But I liked Nurse Bradfield. I thought her a truthful woman, though it was plain she was awfully frightened of Sir Joseph.”

“I never can understand why an honest witness should feel frightened when in the witness-box,” exclaimed Mr. Finch in a tone of contempt.

“Oh, I can understand it so well!” cried the girl. “I know I should be terrified, especially if Sir Joseph were cross-examining me as he cross-examined that poor woman.”

===Chinese===

「我明白了。」伊妮德慢慢地说。

「布拉德菲尔德护士在证人席上的那副光景，给我的印象太深了，」他越说越起劲。「约瑟夫爵士把她吓得语无伦次、魂飞魄散。这是为什么呢？您也许还记得，他怎样逼问她，除了罗杰先生还有谁来过那套公寓；她到最后又怎样只得承认，来过好几位太太和先生，而且她常常压根儿不知道人家是谁！您说，这不是一桩怪事吗？一家之主病倒在床——病得还不至于要请夜班护士，可到底也病得要请一位全天的护士了。可人们照样成群结队地来来去去——来吃午饭，来打桥牌，晚上还来接莱克斯顿太太出门！这在我听来实在蹊跷，简直可以说是可疑。」

「我完全同意，这看起来确实又古怪又狠心，」伊妮德忧心忡忡地说。「可是我喜欢布拉德菲尔德护士。我看她是个说实话的女人，尽管明摆着她把约瑟夫爵士怕得要命。」

「我就是弄不懂，一个诚实的证人上了证人席，有什么可害怕的。」芬奇先生喊道，语气里满是鄙夷。

「哦，我可太能懂了！」姑娘喊道。「我知道我准会吓坏了，尤其是要是约瑟夫爵士像盘问那可怜女人那样，来盘问我。」

===Original===

“Well, be that as it may, Miss Dent, my point is that it came out very clearly that a certain number of men, all friends and admirers of that pretty little lady, came in and out of the flat during the poor chap’s illness.”

“And you actually think that Mrs. Lexton﻿—” and while she was seeking for the right word he broke in with:

“If my theory is correct, I am inclined to think that Mrs. Lexton must have a shrewd suspicion as to who the man was who did that terrible thing﻿—and I shan’t be at all surprised if she makes what is commonly called a good marriage before the year is out!”

“But what an awful thing﻿—to allow an innocent man﻿—”

Again he cut across her words: “She’s a thoroughly selfish woman, and only thinks of herself. But capable of murder?” he shook his head. “Oh no, Miss Dent! The folk who are inclined to think that of her know but little about human nature. I don’t claim to know more of the set Mrs. Lexton lives in than one can gather from the newspapers, and perhaps I ought to say from proceedings in the Bankruptcy Court. But, though they’ll do almost anything for money, those sort of people stop short at murder, believe me.”

===Chinese===

「好吧，就算如此，登特小姐，我的着眼点是：有一点已经摆得明明白白——有那么几个男人，全是那位俊俏的小太太的朋友兼仰慕者，在那可怜人生病期间，进进出出那套公寓。」

「那您竟当真以为，莱克斯顿太太她——」她正斟酌着措辞，他劈头打断：

「倘若我的理论不错，我倒倾向于认为，干出那件可怕之事的男人是谁，莱克斯顿太太心里必定揣着一份透亮的疑猜——而且不等今年过完，她就结上一门世人所谓的好姻缘，我也一点儿不会吃惊！」

「可这是何等可怕的事——任凭一个无辜的人——」

他又一次截断她的话：「她是个彻头彻尾自私的女人，心里只装得下她自己。可要说她下得去手杀人？」他摇了摇头。「哦，不会的，登特小姐！那些爱往这上头想她的人，对人性所知太少。莱克斯顿太太混迹的那个圈子，我不敢说自己比从报纸上——也许我还该说，从破产法院（Bankruptcy Court）的讼案里——能打听到得多知半分。不过这号人虽然为了钱几乎什么都干得出来，到了杀人的门槛前却会收步，相信我。」

===Original===

“Still, you do think it possible that Mrs. Lexton may be shielding a murderer?”

Mr. Finch hesitated. “That’s it exactly. I think she may be shielding a murderer. How did she strike you in the witness-box?”

“I thought her very clever,” said Enid Dent slowly. “Her one object was to produce a good impression, and she succeeded.”

Instinctively Mr. Finch lowered his voice.

“I watched her very carefully, and listened even more carefully, while she was in the witness-box, and I made up my mind that she believes Dr. Gretorex to be an innocent man. Did I say believe? I think she knows he is innocent.”

===Chinese===

「那么，您终究还是认为，莱克斯顿太太有可能在包庇一个杀人凶手？」

芬奇先生迟疑了。「正是如此。我想她也许在包庇一个凶手。她在证人席上给您的观感如何？」

「我觉得她非常聪明，」伊妮德·登特缓缓地说。「她唯一的目标，就是给人留下一个好印象，而她做到了。」

芬奇先生不由自主地放低了声音。

「她在证人席上的时候，我非常仔细地打量她，更加仔细地听她说话，我心里断定：她相信格雷托雷克斯医生是个无辜的人。我说了『相信』吗？我想她是知道他无辜。」

===Original===

“I think another thing,” said Enid, and she, too, allowed her voice to drop.

“What’s that, Miss Dent?” Mr. Finch bent forward.

“I feel quite sure”﻿—and suddenly her pale face became red﻿—“that Roger suspects who did it. I think that’s why he wouldn’t give evidence.”

“God bless my soul,” exclaimed Mr. Finch, “I never thought of that! You may be right, after all. But if it’s true, well, then he’s﻿—”

“Very quixotic?”

“No, Miss Dent. Saving your presence, I was going to say he’s a damn fool.”

===Chinese===

「我还想到另一件事，」伊妮德说，她也放低了声音。

「什么事，登特小姐？」芬奇先生俯身向前。

「我敢断定，」——她苍白的脸倏地涨红了——「罗杰疑心到是谁干的了。我想，这正是他不肯出庭作证的原因。」

「老天爷在上，」芬奇先生失声道，「我从来没往这上头想过！说到底，也许您是对的。可倘若当真如此，那么他就是——」

「太过堂吉诃德了？」

「不，登特小姐。恕我直言，我本想说：他是个天大的傻瓜。」

## XVIII / 十八
===Original===

That same morning, the morning after the conclusion of Gretorex’s trial, Ivy awoke late. For a moment she remembered nothing, for she had gone through a very terrible strain the previous day, a greater strain than she herself had been aware of, at the time.

And then, all at once she remembered﻿—remembered everything, and a sense of something akin to ecstasy flooded her heart. She flung her white arms above her head and stretched herself out luxuriously. Then﻿—for it was very cold﻿—she snuggled down into bed again.

How marvellous to know that her ordeal was over. All over﻿—all over! That from now on she need never see any of the people who had been connected with this awful episode in her life. Perhaps, though, she would make an exception as to Paxton-Smith, for he had been so awfully kind to her, and he admired her so much! His description of how brave and plucky she had been in the witness-box would surely delight Miles Rushworth. Yes, Paxton-Smith should remain her friend.

Meanwhile she would follow his advice. She would go away, that is, to the country for a few days, to the delightful cottage near Brighton belonging to Lady Flora Desmond. She could not go today, unluckily, for Lady Flora had lent the cottage to some tiresome people. But they would be leaving soon, and then she would go down there and have a thorough rest.

===Chinese===

就在同一天早晨——格雷托雷克斯案审判结束后的那个早晨——艾薇醒得很迟。一时之间她什么也不记得了，因为前一天她经受了一场极其可怕的紧张煎熬，那煎熬之重，远超过她当时自己意识到的程度。

接着，蓦地她想起了——想起了一切，一种近乎狂喜的感觉顿时涌上心头。她把雪白的手臂高高抛过头顶，惬意地舒展了身子。然后——因为天很冷——她又缩回被窝里去了。

知道自己的磨难已经结束，这多么美妙啊。全都结束了——全都结束了！从今往后，她再也不必见到那些与她生命中这段可怕插曲有过瓜葛的人了。不过，对帕克斯顿-史密斯，她或许可以破个例，因为他待她实在太好了，而且他又那么仰慕她！他描绘起她在证人席上如何勇敢、如何有胆气的那番话，定会让迈尔斯·拉什沃思听得心花怒放。是的，帕克斯顿-史密斯应该留在她的朋友之列。

眼下她要听从他的忠告。也就是说，她要出门到乡下去待几天，去住弗洛拉·德斯蒙德小姐那座布莱顿近郊的可爱小别墅。不巧的是今天还去不成，因为弗洛拉小姐把别墅借给了几个讨人厌的家伙。好在他们很快就走，到那时她就可以搬过去，痛痛快快地休养一番。

===Original===

Ivy felt she wanted what some of her friends called “a rest cure,” after all she had gone through.

All at once there came a knock on the bedroom door﻿—a knock, and a quick whispered conversation outside. Something, too, very like a giggle.

She called out sharply “Come in,” and the day maid came in with a broad grin on her young face.

“Cook thought maybe that you’d like to see the paper she takes in, ma’am. There’s such a beautiful picture of you in it!”

And on the pink silk eiderdown the maid put down two picture papers, the one that Ivy always glanced at every day, and another paper.

===Chinese===

艾薇觉得，经历了这一切之后，她正需要某些朋友所说的那种「休养疗法」（rest cure）。

冷不防传来一阵敲卧室门的声音——敲了门，门外还响起急促的窃窃私语，而且听来很像是咯咯的笑声。

她厉声喊了句「进来」，日班女仆走了进来，年轻的脸上一副咧到耳根的笑。

「太太，厨娘想着，她说不定您乐意看看她订的那份报。上头有您一张好漂亮的相片呢！」

女仆把两份画报放在粉红色的丝绸鸭绒被上，一份是艾薇每天都要翻看一眼的那份，另一份是别家报纸。

===Original===

Why, yes﻿—there was the picture, and a very good one, too. Ivy had been snapped by a Press photographer just as she had stood on the doorstep of Duke of Kent Mansion, a moment before she got into Paxton-Smith’s car. She gazed with pleasure at all the details of her becoming costume. What a good thing that she had bought that charming little model hat just before poor Jervis’s death. She had soon discovered that what is called “mourning headgear” is apt to be singularly unbecoming.

“It does look nice, ma’am, don’t it? Cook says as how you looked bewitching while you was giving evidence,” ventured the girl.

“I wasn’t thinking of how I was looking,” said Ivy.

And indeed this was the truth. As she had stood up there, the target of all eyes, she had only thought of her coming cross-examination by Sir Joseph Molloy.

===Chinese===

呀，可不是么——相片就在那儿，而且照得真好。那是一位新闻摄影师抓拍的，正当艾薇站在肯特公爵大厦门阶上、上帕克斯顿-史密斯的汽车前的那一刻。她满心欢喜地端详着自己那身衬人的衣装的每一处细节。幸亏她在可怜的杰维斯去世前不久，买下了那顶娇俏的时装原版帽。她很快便发现，所谓「丧服帽饰」，往往格外不衬人。

「照得就是好看，太太，是不是？厨娘说，您作证的时候看着真叫人着迷呢。」女孩子壮着胆子说。

「我当时可没心思琢磨自己看着是什么模样。」艾薇说。

这倒也是实情。她站在那儿、成为全场目光之的时，心里想的只有约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士即将对她进行的那场交叉盘问。

===Original===

And then the girl made a mistake, and she knew that she had done so as soon as she had said the words.

“It does seem sad about that poor Dr. Gretorex, don’t it?” she exclaimed.

For at once Ivy burst into tears﻿—angry, frightened tears. It was too bad, too bad, when she herself had succeeded this morning in entirely banishing Roger from her mind, that he should be thus stupidly, cruelly, thrust into it again.

“Oh, ma’am, I’m so sorry! Please forgive me!” And the tactless young woman almost ran out of the room.

===Chinese===

接着女仆失言了，话一出口她自己便知道说错了。

「可怜的格雷托雷克斯医生落得这个下场，也实在太惨了，是不是？」她冒出这么一句。

艾薇顿时泪如泉涌——又是愤怒、又是惊恐的眼泪。今天早晨她明明已经成功地把罗杰从心里驱赶得一干二净，他却偏又这样愚蠢地、残忍地被硬塞回她脑子里，这实在太狠了，太狠了。

「哎呀，太太，真对不起！请您原谅我！」那个不会看脸色的小女人几乎是逃出了房间。

===Original===

Nurse Bradfield came in to see what was the matter. She looked wan and worn. Unlike Ivy, she had not slept the previous night; unlike Ivy, the face of Roger Gretorex, especially his expression as he had uttered, in answer to the awful question, the quiet words, “Only that I am innocent,” rang in her ears.

She had felt then, and she still felt now, a most painful sensation of doubt.

Was it possible, was it conceivable, that Gretorex was innocent after all, and that her patient had secretly done himself to death?

Every nurse comes across strange and most unexpected happenings in the course of her work. And Nurse Bradfield, though in a sense she had had an uneventful career, had yet been more than once very much startled and surprised by the astonishing things people will sometimes do.

She sat down, now, on the bed, and put her arms round the slender figure, still shaken by angry, frightened sobs.

===Chinese===

布拉德菲尔德护士进来看出了什么事。她面色苍白，一脸倦容。和艾薇不同，她昨夜一夜没合眼；也和艾薇不同，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的那张脸——尤其是他在回答那个可怕的问题时、说出「只是我是清白的」这句平静话语的神情——此刻仍萦回在她耳际。

那时她就怀有、如今依然怀有一种极痛苦的疑虑。

有没有可能，有没有一丝可能，格雷托雷克斯终究是清白的，而她的病人却是悄悄自我了断的呢？

每个护士在职业生涯里都会撞上离奇得出人意料的事。布拉德菲尔德护士虽说一向经历平淡，却也不止一次地被人有时会干出的种种骇人之事，大大地惊到、吓到过。

这时她在床沿坐下，伸臂搂住那具纤瘦的身躯——那身躯还正被愤懑惊恐的抽泣震颤着。

===Original===

“I know how you’re feeling, Mrs. Lexton,” she whispered. “I, too, can’t get Dr. Gretorex out of my mind. But there’s still a chance, you know, that something may be found out, even now. I mean between now and his appeal. Mrs. Berwick told me last night that she knows some great friends of Sir Joseph Molloy, and that he honestly does believe Dr. Gretorex to be innocent. She says that Sir Joseph is going to leave no stone unturned to try to prove his innocence. He’s in a terrible state about it all, and he was very distressed at Dr. Gretorex refusing to give evidence on his own behalf.”

“But you think he did it, don’t you, nurse?”

Ivy lifted her tear-stained eyes and gazed at the older woman.

“I did think so,” muttered Nurse Bradfield. “And even now I can’t see any other explanation. You and I know quite well that Mr. Lexton was not the sort of man to do away with himself.”

“Of course he wasn’t!” exclaimed Ivy, with a touch of indignation.

===Chinese===

「我知道您现在心里是什么滋味，莱克斯顿太太。」她低声说，「我也忘不掉格雷托雷克斯医生。不过您要知道，眼下还有一线希望，说不定还能查出点什么来。我是说，从现在到他上诉（appeal）之前。伯威克太太昨夜告诉我，她认得约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士的几位至交，说他真心相信格雷托雷克斯医生是无辜的。她说约瑟夫爵士打算掀遍每一块石头，竭力证明他的清白。这一切弄得他心焦如焚，而格雷托雷克斯医生不肯为自己出庭作证，也让他非常痛心。」

「可您还是认为是他干的，对不对，护士？」

艾薇抬起泪痕斑斑的眼睛，凝视着这位年长的女人。

「我原先是这样想的。」布拉德菲尔德护士喃喃道，「就是现在，我也看不出还有别的解释。你我都很清楚，莱克斯顿先生不是那种自寻短见的人。」

「他当然不是！」艾薇叫了起来，语气里带着几分愤然。

===Original===

The nurse sighed. “Such extraordinary things do happen in life,” she observed.

“What is it Sir Joseph Molloy thinks he can find out? Did Mrs. Berwick tell you that?” asked Ivy.

She put the question in a careless tone, but she really wanted to know; indeed she was very, very anxious to discover what it was that Sir Joseph Molloy meant to do.

“What he says he means to find out,” said the nurse, “is whether there wasn’t some other person in the world who had a motive for getting rid of Mr. Lexton, besides Dr. Gretorex. He’s got a sort of an idea that there must have been someone else﻿—someone who’s not been thought of yet﻿—someone whose name didn’t appear in the case.”

===Chinese===

护士叹了口气。「人世间，稀奇古怪的事也真会有。」她说。

「约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士觉得他能查出什么来呢？伯威克太太跟您说了吗？」艾薇问。

她问得漫不经心，心里却实在想知道；的确，她非常、非常急于探明约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士究竟打算做什么。

「照他自己的说法，他想要查明的，」护士说，「是这世上除了格雷托雷克斯医生之外，还有没有别人也有除掉莱克斯顿先生的动机。他隐隐觉得，一定还有另一个人——一个至今没人想到过的人——一个名字没有在案子里出现过的人。」

===Original===

And then was heard a hesitating knock on the door, and the maid came in again, looking very much subdued.

On the silver salver lay what had become Ivy’s daily cable from South Africa.

She saw a curious look flash over Nurse Bradfield’s face. As a matter of fact, those daily cables were a source of much interest and speculation to the household, now composed, apart from Ivy, of three women. It was the more mysterious as Mrs. Lexton never left those thick telegrams lying about. The daily cable always disappeared within a comparatively short time of her receipt of the buff-coloured envelope.

Ivy did not open the envelope. She put it on a little table by the side of her bed, and went on talking and listening.

===Chinese===

接着又听得门上怯怯的一阵敲响，女仆再次进来，神情收敛了许多。

银托盘上放着的，是艾薇如今每天一封的南非来电。

她看见一抹古怪的神色从布拉德菲尔德护士脸上一闪而过。事实上，这些每日来电如今已是全家——这个除艾薇外由三个女人组成的家——大感兴趣、竞相揣测的话题。而事情愈发显得神秘的是，莱克斯顿太太从不把那些厚厚的电文随处乱放。每天那封电报，总在她收到牛皮纸信封后没多久，便消失得无影无踪。

艾薇没有拆信封。她把它搁在床边的小桌上，继续又说又听。

===Original===

“Everyone in Court admired the way you gave your evidence, Mrs. Lexton. Mr. Paxton-Smith told me you were the best witness he had ever had. Indeed, he said that you were just perfection! Not too shy, and not too bold. So clear, too! Every word you said could be heard, even where I was sitting.”

And then the speaker added, with considerable heat, “Some of the people there seemed to me like hyenas! Blood﻿—blood﻿—blood﻿—that’s what they wanted, the horrid ghouls! Why, there was a man just behind me who said he hoped that Sir Joseph would make mincemeat of you﻿—”

“I know that some of them wanted that,” murmured Ivy.

“The story goes,” went on Nurse Bradfield, “that Dr. Gretorex begged Sir Joseph to leave you alone.”

===Chinese===

「庭上人人都佩服您作证的样子，莱克斯顿太太。帕克斯顿-史密斯先生告诉我，您是他这辈子遇到过的最好的证人。他还说，您简直是十全十美！不太羞怯，也不太大胆。口齿也那么清楚！您说的每一个字都听得见，连我坐的地方都听得清清楚楚。」

接着说话的人添了一句，火气不小：「那边有些人，在我看来就像一群鬣狗！血——血——血——他们要的就是这个，一群可恶的食尸鬼！嗐，我背后就坐着个男人，说他指望约瑟夫爵士把您剁成肉酱——」

「我知道他们有些人巴不得这样。」艾薇低声道。

「外面有传闻，」布拉德菲尔德护士接着说，「说是格雷托雷克斯医生恳求过约瑟夫爵士，请他放过您。」

===Original===

“I wonder if he did?”

That had not occurred to Ivy. But now, of course, she knew this to be almost certainly the reason Sir Joseph had been so﻿—so unlike what everyone had expected him to be.

And then there did come over her a little glimmer of gratitude. Yes, Roger certainly loved her. No one would ever care for her as he cared. She remembered, now, his having once said that he would go through any torture in order to save her a moment’s pain. Well? Poor Roger hadn’t really gone through torture exactly﻿—that sort of thing has been given up long ago, luckily. Still, it was very touching that, even in his own time of danger, he had thought of her and of her reputation.

After Nurse Bradfield had left the room, and after Ivy’s light breakfast had been brought in and arranged on the bed-table, she broke open Rushworth’s cable.

===Chinese===

「真不知他是不是这样做了？」

艾薇此前倒没想到这一层。可现在她自然明白了，这几乎肯定就是约瑟夫爵士何以那般——那般大异于众人所料的缘由。

于是她心里确实掠过了一丝感激。是的，罗杰当然是爱她的。再没有人会像他那样疼她了。她此刻想起，他曾说过，为了使她免受片刻的痛楚，什么样的酷刑他都甘愿承受。嗯，可又如何呢？可怜的罗杰其实并没有真吃什么酷刑——幸好那一套早就废除了。尽管如此，即便在自身难保的关头，他心里还惦记着她、惦记着她的名声，这仍然十分动人。

布拉德菲尔德护士离开房间后，艾薇的清淡早餐也送了进来，在床桌上摆好，她这才拆开拉什沃思的电报。

===Original===

> My sister died yesterday. Sailing for home the day after tomorrow. Will keep you advised by wireless of exact date of my return. I have been thinking of you night and day.

Rushworth coming back now, almost at once? Small wonder that a feeling of ecstasy flooded Ivy Lexton’s whole being. She had gone through a terrible ordeal, but that which was already in sight would make up for everything.

She jumped out of bed and locked her door. Then she went over to the fireplace, and watched the flimsy sheets curl up and become thin and black in the flames. After the first, she had always burnt each of Rushworth’s cables as soon as she had read it through. Somehow it seemed to her safer to do so.

Unlocking the door, she rang for the maid to put on her bath, for she wanted to go out and telegraph to Miles Rushworth.

===Chinese===

> 舍妹昨日去世。后天启航回国。归期确切日期，当以无线电报奉告。我日夜都在思念你。

拉什沃思这就回来了，几乎立刻就回来了？难怪一阵狂喜漫过了艾薇·莱克斯顿的整个身心。她经受了一场可怕的磨难，但已然在望的那桩事，将把一切都补偿回来。

她跳下床，锁上房门。然后走到壁炉边，看着那几页薄薄的电报纸在火焰里蜷曲起来，烧成又薄又黑的灰。打头一封之后，拉什沃思的每一封电报，她都是一读完便即刻烧掉。不知怎么，她总觉得这样更稳妥些。

开了锁，她按铃叫女仆放洗澡水，因为她要出门去给迈尔斯·拉什沃思发电报。

---

===Original===

It was half-past ten when Ivy came back to the flat.

“There’s a young lady to see you in the drawing-room, ma’am,” said the maid.

Ivy walked into the room smiling, for she expected to see waiting for her one of the many women belonging to her old, idle, easy life. Why shouldn’t they go out together shopping, and then come back to lunch?

But the smile froze on her face, for it was a stranger who rose and confronted her. Certainly a stranger, and yet somehow she had a disturbing feeling that she had seen her visitor before, and in disagreeable circumstances.

Then all at once, with a feeling of sharp annoyance, she realised that this was the girl who had been sitting with Mrs. Gretorex during the concluding hours of Roger’s trial yesterday. And when she, Ivy, and Roger’s mother had met face to face in a corridor of the Old Bailey, the stranger had been there too.

===Chinese===

艾薇回到公寓时，已是十点半钟。

「客厅里有位年轻小姐要见您，太太。」女仆说。

艾薇满面笑容地走进客厅，因为她料想着等她的，必是她昔日那种闲散安逸生活里的众多女友中的一个。她们何不一起出门购物，再回来共进午餐呢？

可笑容在她脸上凝住了，因为站起身来面对她的，是个陌生女子。诚然是个陌生人，可不知怎的，她心里隐隐不安，觉得自己从前见过这位来客，而且是在不愉快的场合。

随即，她怀着尖锐的恼怒蓦地认了出来：这正是昨天罗杰审判的最后几个钟头里，陪伴格雷托雷克斯太太坐在一旁的那个姑娘。而当她艾薇与罗杰的母亲在老贝利的一条走廊里打照面时，这个陌生人也在场。

===Original===

“I hope you’ll forgive my coming in this way without having first asked if you would see me,” said Enid Dent. “But the matter is very urgent, Mrs. Lexton, and the time is short, very short, between now and Roger Gretorex’s appeal, otherwise I feel sure Mrs. Gretorex would have come herself. Unfortunately, she is ill today.”

As Ivy still said nothing, only looked at her with an expression of fear, and yes, of dislike, on her lovely face, Enid exclaimed desperately, “I am sure you would do anything to help Roger Gretorex, Mrs. Lexton?”

And then Ivy did what all through her life she had often done, when in doubt. She burst into tears.

“Of course, I’d do anything,” she sobbed, “anything I could do! But what can I do? I’ve gone through such an awful time. No one knows what I’ve gone through, or how miserable I’ve been. No one thinks of me!” she ended hysterically. “I feel as if I hadn’t a friend in the world﻿—”

===Chinese===

「我没有先问您愿不愿见我，就这样径直进来了，望您原谅。」伊妮德·登特说，「只是事情万分紧急，莱克斯顿太太，从现在到罗杰·格雷托雷克斯上诉，时间太仓促、太仓促了，不然我确信格雷托雷克斯太太一定会亲自来的。遗憾的是，她今天病倒了。」

见艾薇仍一言不发，只拿那张可爱的脸望着她，脸上带着惧意——是的，还有厌恶——伊妮德绝望地脱口喊道：「您一定愿意尽一切力量帮助罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的，对吗，莱克斯顿太太？」

于是艾薇使出了她一辈子在拿不定主意时常使的那一手——放声大哭起来。

「我当然愿意做任何事，」她抽抽噎噎地哭着，「凡我做得到的，什么都愿意！可我又能做什么呢？我受了多大的罪啊。没人知道我受了什么罪，也没人知道我有多苦。没有一个人想着我！」末了她歇斯底里地嚷道，「我觉得这世上我连一个朋友都没有——」

===Original===

Enid went up close to her, and touched her on the arm.

“I’m so sorry,” she said in a troubled tone. “I know how terrible it must have been for you yesterday.”

She felt ashamed of what she had been led to believe by Mr. Finch an hour ago. It seemed incredible to her that the poor little creature before her, now trembling with emotion, could have acted the cruel part Alfred Finch and Sir Joseph believed she was acting, shielding the real murderer of her husband, and condemning an innocent man to a frightful death.

Ivy saw that she had made a good impression, and she became gradually calm.

===Chinese===

伊妮德走到她跟前，碰了碰她的手臂。

「我真难过。」她说，声调不安，「我知道昨天对您来说一定可怕极了。」

她为自己一小时前被阿尔弗雷德·芬奇引得相信的那套说法感到羞愧。在她看来，眼前这个此刻正激动得发抖的可怜小人儿，竟可能在扮演阿尔弗雷德·芬奇和约瑟夫爵士相信她扮演的那个残忍角色——包庇杀害她丈夫的真凶，把一个无辜的男人送上可怕的死路——这简直是不可思议的。

艾薇见自己给人留下了好印象，便渐渐平静下来。

===Original===

Her one object was to get rid of this tiresome girl quietly. It had been stupid, very stupid, of the maid to allow a stranger to come in and wait, without knowing anything of her business. After all, this girl didn’t look in the least like one of her, Ivy’s, smart friends. Enid looked, to her critic’s practised eyes, a country bumpkin dressed in a plain and by no means expensive, if well-cut, coat and skirt.

“I suppose,” she said politely, “that you’re poor Mrs. Gretorex’s companion?”

“Yes,” answered Enid, “I am her companion. I’ve known her all my life; and I’m very, very sorry for her.” And then her voice, too, broke.

“What is it that Mrs. Gretorex thinks I can do?” asked Ivy in a timorous voice.

As the girl, who was struggling with her tears, answered nothing to this: “Of course I’d do anything if I thought it could be of any good,” she concluded.

===Chinese===

她的唯一目的，就是不声不响地打发走这个讨厌的姑娘。女仆放一个陌生人进来等候、又不问清来意，实在愚蠢，太愚蠢了。说到底，这姑娘看上去丝毫不像她艾薇那些时髦朋友中的一员。在这位品评者的老练眼里，伊妮德活脱是个乡下丫头，穿一身素净的、绝说不上昂贵的上衣配裙——尽管剪裁还算合体。

「我猜，」她客客气气地说，「您是可怜的格雷托雷克斯太太的陪伴女伴吧？」

「是的，」伊妮德答道，「我是她的陪伴女伴。我打小就认识她；我非常、非常为她难过。」说到这里，她的嗓音也哽咽了。

「格雷托雷克斯太太觉得，我能做些什么呢？」艾薇怯怯地问。

那姑娘正强忍着眼泪，没有作答，于是她又补上一句：「当然，只要我觉得真能有点用处，我什么都愿意做。」

===Original===

And then, suddenly, she had an inspiration.

“People seem to forget all about poor Jervis,” she said in a hurt tone. “After all, he was my husband, and I was very fond of him, Miss﻿—?”

“Dent,” said the other quietly. “My name is Enid Dent.”

And then she moved a little farther away from the still fur-clad little figure, for those words, uttered in so pathetic a tone, had suddenly brought Roger before Enid Dent. Roger, God help him, had loved, perhaps still loved, this woman.

“Well, Miss Dent, no one ever thinks now about poor Jervis, do they?”

===Chinese===

跟着，她灵机一动。

「大家好像把可怜的杰维斯忘得一干二净了。」她用委屈的口气说，「不管怎么说，他是我丈夫，我是很爱他的，小姐——您怎么称呼？」

「登特。」对方平静地说，「我叫伊妮德·登特。」

说完她便朝旁边挪开了些，离那具仍旧裹着皮裘的娇小身影远了些，因为那几句说得如此凄楚的话，忽然把罗杰带到了伊妮德·登特的眼前。罗杰——上帝保佑他——曾经爱过，也许至今还爱着这个女人。

「您瞧，登特小姐，如今谁也不再想着可怜的杰维斯了，是不是？」

===Original===

That had been a remark made to Ivy by Paxton-Smith a few days ago, and she had been struck by the truth of it.

Enid felt a tremor of discomfort flash across her burdened heart. It was quite true that though his mysterious death had formed the subject of a great and searching inquiry, none of them, now, gave any thought to Jervis Lexton, the unfortunate young man who had certainly been poisoned by someone masquerading as a friend.

“I do know how you must feel about that,” she said in a low voice. “But it’s only natural for Mrs. Gretorex, and the friends of Roger Gretorex, to be thinking of him rather than of your husband, Mrs. Lexton. You see, we who have known Roger all his life, are absolutely convinced that he is innocent.”

And then Ivy, whose nerves were on edge, suddenly made, in her own immediate interest, a mistake.

===Chinese===

这话本是几天前帕克斯顿-史密斯对艾薇说的，当时她就觉得此言不虚。

伊妮德感到一阵不安的颤栗，掠过她那颗不堪重负的心。的确，虽然他的离奇死亡曾是一场轰动一时、细究深查的研讯的主题，如今他们这些人里，却没有一个还想着杰维斯·莱克斯顿——这个不幸的青年，分明是被某个假扮朋友的人毒害的。

「您心里的滋味，我完全明白。」她低声说，「可是格雷托雷克斯太太和罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的朋友们心里想的是他，而不是您丈夫，这也是人之常情，莱克斯顿太太。您知道，我们这些看着罗杰长大的人，都笃信他是无辜的。」

这时艾薇本已神经紧绷，出于眼前的利害，突然犯了一个错误。

===Original===

“Everyone *I* see,” she said quickly, defensively, “feels quite sure that Roger Gretorex did do it. You must know that, Miss Dent, though of course I wouldn’t say so to his mother.”

“Does that mean that you”﻿—Enid Dent took a step forward, and the other instinctively stepped back as she met the accusing look in the girl’s eyes﻿—“yourself are convinced of his guilt, Mrs. Lexton?”

“I don’t think you have a right to ask me such a question!” She uttered the rebuke lightly, pettishly.

Why, oh! why, didn’t this tiresome, disagreeable girl go away? She had no business here. Besides, she was only a paid companion. Ivy had a great contempt for any woman earning her own living in a quiet, humdrum way.

A tide of anger was rising up in her heart, making her what she seldom was, really angry.

===Chinese===

「我见到的每一个人，」她急急地、带着辩白的口气说，「都确信就是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯干的。这一点您肯定知道，登特小姐，虽然我当然不会当他母亲的面这么说。」

「那么，这话是不是说，您」——伊妮德·登特向前跨了一步，对方迎上姑娘眼中那谴责的目光，不由自主地退了一步——「您本人也确信他有罪呢，莱克斯顿太太？」

「我看您没有权利问我这样的问题！」这句责备，她轻轻吐出，带着愠怒。

为什么，唉，为什么这个讨厌的、不识趣的姑娘还不走？她在这里毫无待着的道理。再说了，她不过是个拿薪水的陪伴女伴。艾薇素来鄙夷一切安分守己、按部就班自己谋生的女人。

一股怒潮在她心头升起，使她成了她平素难得的样子——当真动怒了。

===Original===

“It’s a hideous misfortune for me that I ever met Roger Gretorex!” she exclaimed. “And yet you heard what I said in the witness-box? I did try, indeed I did, to help Dr. Gretorex. What is more﻿—”

Enid had moved away again. She was standing still now, a look of despair on her face.

“﻿—Mr. Paxton-Smith told me I oughtn’t to say a word, and I promised him that I wouldn’t say a word to anyone ever, unless he was there too!”

Anger is very catching, as most of us know, and wrath had also risen up in Enid Dent’s heart. How agonising it was to know that it was this cruel, foolish, selfish, silly woman who had stolen the man she, Enid, loved, and who, she believed in her heart, had loved her, before some malign fate had thrown him in Mrs. Lexton’s way.

So it was that, when she saw Ivy begin sidling towards the door, with a quick movement she flung herself across the room and stood with her back against it, barring the way.

===Chinese===
