「我认识罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，简直是我倒霉透顶的大祸事！」她嚷道，「可您也听见我在证人席上说的话了吧？我确实尽了力，真的尽了力，想帮格雷托雷克斯医生的忙。不但如此——」

伊妮德又挪开了些。此刻她一动不动地站着，脸上是一片绝望的神情。

「——不但如此，帕克斯顿-史密斯先生叮嘱过我一个字也不该说，我也向他保证了，往后不管对谁我一个字也不说，除非他也在场！」

愤怒是极容易传染的，这我们大都知道；怒火也已经升上了伊妮德·登特的心头。明知夺走她——伊妮德——所爱的男人的，正是这个残忍、愚蠢、自私、糊涂的女人；而她心底相信，若不是某种恶毒的命运把这个男人掷到莱克斯顿太太的道上，他本是爱她的——这份明白，是何等的煎熬。

于是，当她看见艾薇开始侧着身子往门口蹭时，她一个箭步横穿过房间，背抵着门站定，堵住了去路。

===Original===

“Mrs. Lexton?”

She uttered the other’s name calmly, though she was now shaking all over.

“Yes, Miss Dent? I’m sure no good can come of our going on talking﻿—”

“Not only do I believe, but certain people, whose opinion you no doubt would value far more than mine, believe too, that you know Roger Gretorex to be innocent!” cried Enid. “They are convinced that you are well aware who it was who craftily, cruelly, secretly poisoned your husband. I warn you here and now, that if that is true, the truth is going to be discovered!”

She stopped, ashamed of, and frightened at, her own emotion. She felt now, as if it were someone else who had uttered that passionate warning.

===Chinese===

「莱克斯顿太太？」

她平静地唤出对方的名字，尽管她此刻浑身发抖。

「什么事，登特小姐？我相信，我们再谈下去也不会有什么好结果——」

「不只是我相信，还有一些人——他们的见解，您无疑看得远比我的重——也同样相信，您明知罗杰·格雷托雷克斯是无辜的！」伊妮德喊道，「他们确信，是谁狡诈地、残忍地、暗中毒死了您丈夫，您心里一清二楚。我在此当面警告您：倘若真是这样，真相终究会被查个水落石出！」

她停住了，为自己的激动既羞愧又心惊。此刻她觉得，方才喊出那句激愤警告的，仿佛是另一个人。

===Original===

Ivy Lexton suddenly gave a stifled cry. Tottering forward she sank down on a chair, and, moaning, covered her face with her hands.

All at once, she could not have said why, perhaps it was a glimpse she caught of Ivy Lexton’s convulsed face, there flashed into the girl’s mind a certain dread suspicion; and much that had seemed inexplicable suddenly became clear.

Ivy slipped off the chair on to the floor, and lay there quite still.

Enid Dent opened the door.

“I am afraid,” she said quietly to the maid who had been in the hall obviously listening to what was going on inside the drawing-room, “that Mrs. Lexton has fainted.”

===Chinese===

艾薇·莱克斯顿突然发出一声憋在喉头的喊叫。她踉跄着往前挪，跌坐在一把椅子上，双手掩面，呻吟着。

蓦地——她说不清是为什么，也许是她瞥见了艾薇·莱克斯顿那张扭曲变形的脸——一个可怕的疑团闪进了姑娘的脑海；许多原本显得无从解释的事，顿时豁然明朗。

艾薇从椅子上滑落到地板上，直挺挺地躺在那儿，一动不动。

伊妮德·登特打开了门。

「恐怕，」她平静地对那个女仆说——那女仆分明一直待在门厅里，偷听着客厅里的动静，「莱克斯顿太太晕过去了。」

===Original===

As the scared-looking young woman went to call Nurse Bradfield, who was then packing, for she was about to go on to a new case, Enid left the flat and, without waiting for the lift, she ran down the stairs.

Hailing a taxicab she threw the driver her address in Ebury Street. She felt extraordinarily excited, carried out of herself. Consciously she longed for the man who was driving her to go faster﻿—faster! At last he drew up; she paid him off, put the latchkey in the lock, and then, shaking with excitement, she walked straight into the room where Mrs. Gretorex was still lying back in the big armchair.

“I think I know now,” she said in a stifled voice, “who poisoned Jervis Lexton, and you and I, Mrs. Gretorex, must try and think of a way in which we can get proof, proof﻿—proof!”

Mrs. Gretorex looked up at the girl.

“Who is it you now suspect?” she asked slowly, “of having poisoned Jervis Lexton?”

Enid hesitated for a moment, then she said in a low voice, “His wife.”

===Chinese===

那个面带惊惶的年轻女人去叫布拉德菲尔德护士——护士当时正在收拾行装，因为她就要去接一桩新的看护差事——伊妮德则离开了公寓，不等电梯，径自顺着楼梯跑了下去。

她拦下一辆出租汽车，向司机报了伊伯里街的住址。她只觉异常亢奋，整个人像被抽离了自身。她一门心思盼着载她的这个人开快些——再快些！车子终于停了下来；她付了车钱，把弹簧锁钥匙插进锁孔，然后激动得浑身发抖，径直走进那个房间——格雷托雷克斯太太仍旧仰靠在那张大扶手椅里。

「我想我现在知道了，」她声音哽塞地说，「是谁毒死了杰维斯·莱克斯顿。您和我，格雷托雷克斯太太，得想法子找出个法子，拿到证据，证据——证据！」

格雷托雷克斯太太抬眼望着姑娘。

「你如今疑心的是谁？」她缓缓问道，「疑心是谁毒死了杰维斯·莱克斯顿？」

伊妮德迟疑了片刻，然后低声说：「他的妻子。」

===Original===

“I have felt almost sure, from the first, that Ivy Lexton poisoned her husband,” said Mrs. Gretorex quietly.

Then she rose and, coming quite close up to the girl, she added: “What is more, I am convinced that Roger knows the truth now. That is the real reason why he begged Sir Joseph Molloy to be very careful as to what questions he put to Mrs. Lexton in his cross-examination.”

Mrs. Gretorex took Enid’s hand.

“You and I believe this terrible thing of Ivy Lexton. But it can do Roger no good to say what we believe. It would, even, probably, do him harm.”

===Chinese===

「我从一开始就几乎笃定，是艾薇·莱克斯顿毒死了她的丈夫。」格雷托雷克斯太太平静地说。

说着她站起身来，走到离姑娘极近的地方，又道：「不但如此，我确信罗杰如今已经知道真相了。这才是他恳求约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士、要他在交叉盘问莱克斯顿太太时务必谨慎择问的真正缘由。」

格雷托雷克斯太太握住伊妮德的手。

「你我都相信，艾薇·莱克斯顿干下了这桩可怕的事。可是把我们的想法说出去，对罗杰没有半点好处。甚至，多半还会害了他。」

===Original===

“Does Mr. Oram know that you think her guilty?”

“Yes,” said Roger’s mother, and she sighed. “I told him just before the trial opened. He begged me most earnestly to put any idea of the kind out of my mind, as he felt convinced I was wrong. He pointed out to me that there was not what he called an iota of evidence connecting Mrs. Lexton with the crime. In fact, I saw that I dropped very very much in his estimation as a sensible woman when I told him of my more than suspicion, of my absolute conviction, that Mrs. Lexton had had some all-powerful motive for wishing her husband dead.”

“Surely we can discover what that was?”

Mrs. Gretorex shook her head. “She certainly did not wish to marry Roger﻿—of that I feel quite sure. I hoped against hope that something might come out while Sir Joseph Molloy cross-examined her. But, of course, nothing did come out, and it is my conviction, Enid, that nothing ever will.”

===Chinese===

「奥拉姆先生知道您认为她有罪吗？」

「知道。」罗杰的母亲说，叹了口气，「开庭前不久我告诉了他。他极其恳切地求我把这类念头统统从脑子里赶出去，因为他笃信是我错了。他向我指出，用他的话说，连一丝一毫将莱克斯顿太太与罪行牵连起来的证据都没有。说实在的，我看得出，当我说出我不止是怀疑、而是笃信莱克斯顿太太必有什么压倒一切的动机、巴望她丈夫死掉的时候，我在他心目中作为一个明白事理的女人的分量，已经一落千丈了。」

「那动机究竟是什么，我们总归查得出来吧？」

格雷托雷克斯太太摇了摇头。「她肯定不是想要嫁给罗杰——这一点我很有把握。约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士盘问她的时候，我曾抱着万一的指望，盼着能问出点什么来。可是，什么也没问出来，而且我笃信，伊妮德，往后也永远问不出来。」

---

===Original===

That same day the fact that Roger Gretorex had made up his mind not to appeal appeared in the late editions of the evening papers. “It will only prolong the agony for my mother,” he had said to Mr. Oram. “And as for me, I am sufficiently a coward to long for it to be all over.”

And so they all﻿—those who loved Roger, and she who feared him, together with the myriads of men and women who regarded him as a callous murderer, and who hoped that he would finally confess his crime﻿—waited for the end.

===Chinese===

就在同一天，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯已决意不再上诉的消息，登在了晚报的晚版上。「这只会延长我母亲的煎熬，」他对奥拉姆先生说，「至于我，我算得上够懦弱的了，只盼这一切早点了结。」

于是他们所有人——那些爱着罗杰的人，和那个惧怕他的人，连同把他视为冷酷凶手、巴望他最终认罪的无数男男女女——都在等待着结局。

## XX / 二十
===Original===

The morning after these events had taken place, the Home Secretary, Sir Edward Law, was moving about his fine room in Whitehall. He felt restless and thoroughly ill at ease, and that, although he was a statesman noted for his calm and cool temperament.

Within a few moments from now he expected his door to open and three persons to be shown in. First there would be a solicitor named John Oram, whose name he vaguely knew as that of a man of the highest standing in his profession, and who, the year before, had been President of the Law Society. Mr. Oram was the legal adviser of Roger Gretorex, a man convicted of murder, whose execution had been fixed to take place the following morning at nine o’clock. Then Sir Joseph Molloy, the most famous advocate of the day, known by the cynically minded as “the murderer’s friend,” who had defended Roger Gretorex at the Old Bailey would accompany Mr. Oram, though his presence could not be regarded as being quite in order. However, Sir Joseph was a very old friend of the Home Secretary, and he had pleaded urgently to be allowed to come this morning. The Judge, Mr. Justice Mayhew, who had tried Roger Gretorex, was the third visitor expected, his presence at the forthcoming conference being, very properly, regarded as essential.

===Chinese===

这些事发生后的第二天早晨，内政大臣（Home Secretary）爱德华·劳爵士（Sir Edward Law）正在白厅（Whitehall）他那间雅致的房间里踱来踱去。他心神不定，浑身上下都不自在——尽管他是位以沉着冷静著称的政治家。

再过片刻，他就要听见房门打开、三个人被引进来了。头一位是位名叫约翰·奥拉姆的律师——这名字他隐约知道，是业内声望极高的人物，前一年还做过律师公会（Law Society）会长。奥拉姆先生是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的法律顾问；此人已被判谋杀罪名成立，行刑定于次日早晨九点执行。然后是约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士——当今最负盛名的出庭律师，被惯于冷眼看世的人谑称为「凶手之友」，曾在老贝利为罗杰·格雷托雷克斯辩护——他将陪同奥拉姆先生前来，尽管他的到场未免有些不合规矩。不过，约瑟夫爵士是内政大臣的至交老友，再三恳求今天上午容他同来。第三位要来的，是审理过罗杰·格雷托雷克斯一案的梅休法官；即将举行的这次会商，他理当到场，乃是万不可缺之事。

===Original===

An odd thing had happened only the previous day in connection with this Gretorex case. Sir Edward Law had received an envelope, marked “Private,” and containing a letter signed “Roger Gretorex.” With it, a plain piece of paper bore the following words: “The enclosed was written to Mrs. Lexton only last November, after the beginning of Jervis Lexton’s illness. It reads like the letter of an innocent man.”

That touching, in its way noble, love-letter had much impressed him, and had added a note of real mystery to a story with all the details of which he was by now painfully familiar.

===Chinese===

就在前一天，这桩格雷托雷克斯案又出了一桩怪事。爱德华·劳爵士收到一只信封，上面标着「亲启」，里面装着一封署名「罗杰·格雷托雷克斯」的信。随信另附一页素笺，写着如下字句：「内附之信写于去年十一月，即杰维斯·莱克斯顿发病之后，收信人为莱克斯顿太太。此信读来，像是一个无辜之人所写。」

那封情书动人，甚至有几分高贵，给他留下了极深的印象，也给这个故事添上了一笔真正的神秘色彩——而其中的种种细节，他如今已是痛苦地了然于胸了。

===Original===

At last Sir Edward stopped in front of his writing-table. There, in a place by themselves, stood five white cards. Each was marked with a name and a date; and they formed a perpetual reminder that four men and one woman were now lying under sentence of death. For the date on each of those death-cards was the day on which the person named was to suffer the last penalty of the law.

The Home Secretary’s eyes became fixed on the card bearing the name of Roger Gretorex, the young man of gentle birth who had been sentenced to death at the Old Bailey for the murder of one Jervis Lexton. And, as he gazed at the rather unusual name, the Minister, in whose hands the fate of these men and one woman still reposed, asked himself, with a tightening of the heart, whether Sir Joseph Molloy might not be right after all in his belief that there had been a grave miscarriage of justice.

Sir Edward Law was a man with a high sense of duty. At first he had naturally accepted the verdict at the trial as conclusive of Gretorex’s guilt, and he had daily expected to hear the news that there had been a full confession, especially after he learnt that the condemned man had refused to enter an appeal. But he had been unwillingly impressed by Sir Joseph Molloy’s strong conviction of his client’s innocence, and now he understood that certain extraordinary new evidence was to be laid before him this morning, at what was indeed the eleventh hour.

===Chinese===

爱德华爵士终于在写字台前站定。台上一处单独的位置，立着五张白色卡片。每张卡片上都标着一个名字和一个日期；它们构成一种常在的提醒：如今有四个男人和一个女人正处于死刑判决之下。因为那一张张死亡卡片上的日期，正是卡片上具名之人遭受法律最后极刑的日子。

内政大臣的目光，落在了写着罗杰·格雷托雷克斯名字的那张卡片上——这个出身良家的青年，因谋杀一个名叫杰维斯·莱克斯顿的人，已在老贝利被判处死刑。他凝视着这个颇为少见的姓氏，这位仍执掌着这几个男人和那个女人命运的部长，心头一阵发紧，暗自问自己：约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士坚信此案铸成了大冤案，莫非他终究是对的？

爱德华·劳爵士是个责任感极强的人。起初，他自然把庭审的裁决当作格雷托雷克斯罪行的定论，尤其是听说那死刑犯拒绝上诉之后，他天天都指望着传来全面认罪的消息。但约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士对其当事人无辜所抱的坚定信念，不由得令他动容；如今他明白，今天上午将有一桩异乎寻常的新证据摆到他面前——而且当真是在最后关头了。

===Original===

That was why, as late as the day before, the Home Secretary had conscientiously read once more all the documents, and they were many, connected with what had been called “The Lexton Mystery.” He had felt it to be his plain duty thus to prepare himself for the critical examination which it would be his business to apply to this new evidence.

And yet? And yet, he could not imagine what new evidence could possibly be adduced of a nature strong enough to upset the apparently conclusive case built up against Roger Gretorex at the trial.

===Chinese===

正因如此，直到前一天，内政大臣还一丝不苟地把全部文件——数量众多——凡是与那桩被称为「莱克斯顿疑案」的案子有关的，又从头至尾读了一遍。他觉得，如此为自己备好功课，以待对这桩新证据施行严格审读，是他义不容辞的责任。

然而呢？然而，他想不出还能提出什么样的新证据，其分量足以推翻庭审时针对罗杰·格雷托雷克斯构筑起来的那套看似铁证如山的罪案。

---

===Original===

The door opened, and Sir Edward’s principal private secretary came in.

“Sir Joseph Molloy to see you, sir, by appointment. And there is another man with him.”

Thus announced, Sir Joseph Molloy, who was followed by Alfred Finch, entered the room and, after greeting his old friend, the Home Secretary, came at once to business.

“Mr. Oram is unfortunately ill, so I have ventured to bring in his stead his head clerk, Mr. Finch, who has had all the threads of the Gretorex case in his hands. Indeed, it is to Mr. Finch that I believe we owe the proof of a fearful miscarriage of justice. I hope he will be able to convince you, Sir Edward, of the innocence of his most unfortunate client, Roger Gretorex. ‘Murder, though it hath no tongue, will speak!’ ” added Sir Joseph in a dramatic tone.

===Chinese===

房门开了，爱德华爵士的首席私人秘书走了进来。

「约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士按约前来见您，先生。与他同来的还有一位。」

通报完毕，约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士走了进来，身后跟着阿尔弗雷德·芬奇。他与老友内政大臣寒暄过后，立刻谈起了正事。

「不巧奥拉姆先生病了，所以我冒昧代他带来了他的主任书记员芬奇先生——格雷托雷克斯案的一应头绪，全都捏在他手里。老实说，我相信正是多亏了芬奇先生，我们才拿到了这桩可怕冤案的证明。我希望他能让您信服，爱德华爵士，信服他那位最不幸的当事人罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的清白。『谋杀虽无舌，自会开口言！』」约瑟夫爵士又用戏剧腔添了一句。

===Original===

The Home Secretary slightly raised his eyebrows. Sir Joseph was going just a little bit too fast, as he sometimes did, especially when he had any kind of audience. But the famous advocate realised that he was not going quite the right way to work, for quickly he changed his tone:

“I think, Sir Edward, that after you have seen the statutory declaration made by a certain person who was closely connected with Gretorex’s London life, as well as other new evidence which Mr. Finch is about to lay before you, you will agree that there is a strong case for, at any rate, the postponement of Roger Gretorex’s execution.”

And then the door of the room opened again, and the Judge who had tried the Lexton case came in.

Mr. Justice Mayhew appeared outwardly his usual calm and dignified self. But within he was full of interest, and even a certain excitement. Unlike the Home Secretary, he thought nothing of Sir Joseph Molloy’s belief in his client’s innocence; what had profoundly impressed him had been the condemned man’s refusal to appeal.

===Chinese===

内政大臣微微扬起了眉毛。约瑟夫爵士未免走得太快了，他有时就是这样，尤其周围有看客的时候。但这位大名鼎鼎的出庭律师也意识到自己这一套不大对路，赶紧换了口气：

「爱德华爵士，我想，等您看过了由一位与格雷托雷克斯伦敦生活有密切牵连之人所作的法定声明（statutory declaration），再加上芬奇先生即将呈请您过目的其他新证据，您就会同意：无论如何，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的行刑，有充分理由暂缓执行。」

这时房门又开了，审理莱克斯顿案的那位法官走了进来。

梅休法官表面上仍是平日那副安详庄重的模样。可他内心满是兴味，甚至有几分激动。与内政大臣不同，他对约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士笃信当事人无辜一事不以为意；真正令他深受触动的，是那死刑犯拒绝上诉。

===Original===

A few moments later the three men﻿—for Alfred Finch was standing a little aside, he had done his part and he knew the documents which he had brought with him almost by heart﻿—were gazing with intense curiosity at Mrs. Huntley’s statutory declaration. Each, in turn, read the pasted-up fragments of Ivy Lexton’s two passionate love-letters. They belonged to an early period of her friendship with Roger Gretorex, and each letter proposed a meeting at Ferry Place. On each occasion she had chosen an evening, or rather a night, when her husband was to be with an old friend who had a fishing place some way from London.

And then the Home Secretary took out of a drawer, and handed to the Judge, Gretorex’s own piteous letter to Ivy Lexton, the letter which had remained so long hidden in Mrs. Berwick’s desk.

It took quite a little while for Sir Edward Law and Mr. Justice Mayhew to make themselves fully acquainted with what had been laid before them. And then they looked at one another in silence for a moment. As for Sir Joseph, he wisely said nothing, though he was longing intensely to express something of the triumph and exultation which filled his heart.

===Chinese===

片刻之后，这三个人——阿尔弗雷德·芬奇则稍站一旁，他已经尽了自己的本分，带来的那些文件他几乎能倒背如流——便怀着强烈的好奇，端详起亨特利太太的法定声明来。他们依次读了艾薇·莱克斯顿那两封情火炽烈的情书拼贴而成的碎片。那是她与罗杰·格雷托雷克斯交情的初期，两封信都是约在费里街相会。每一次她挑的都是这样一个夜晚——不如说这样一个深夜：她丈夫要陪一位老友度过，那位老友在离伦敦有段路程的地方有一处垂钓的去处。

接着，内政大臣从抽屉里取出格雷托雷克斯写给艾薇·莱克斯顿的那封凄恻的信，递给法官——正是那封在伯威克太太书桌里藏了许久的信。

爱德华·劳爵士和梅休法官花了相当一阵工夫，才把摆到面前的这些东西全盘弄清。随后两人默默对视了片刻。至于约瑟夫爵士，他很识趣地一言不发，尽管他心里正燃烧着渴望，想把满腔的胜利与狂喜一吐为快。

===Original===

“I read a full report of the case over again yesterday,” said the Home Secretary. “There seemed to me, then, no doubt as to the guilt of Roger Gretorex. But this Mrs. Huntley’s report of what she swears she saw the very day before, it is now ascertained, Jervis Lexton had his first attack of illness, does, I admit, entirely alter the complexion of everything. But I should not have attached very great importance to a statement which rests on the word of one person, who, if she tells the truth now, certainly lied before, had we not also these three letters. They prove that Mrs. Lexton has committed gross perjury.”

As the two men he was addressing remained silent, he went on: “I suppose the police made a thorough search of the flat in which Jervis Lexton met his death?”

And then all at once Alfred Finch took a hand.

“No, Sir Edward, the flat was not searched,” he answered deferentially.

===Chinese===

「昨天我又把全案的完整报道读了一遍。」内政大臣说。「当时在我看来，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的罪行毫无疑义。可是这位亨特利太太，她宣誓声称亲眼所见之事，就发生在——如今查明——杰维斯·莱克斯顿初次发病的前一天；她这份证词，我承认，的确使全案的面貌为之彻底改观了。不过，倘若单单是这样一份证词——只凭一人之词，这人若如今讲的是实话，先前就必定撒过谎——我本不会十分看重；但我们手里还有这三封信。它们证明，莱克斯顿太太犯了严重的伪证罪。」

他面前的两人都沉默不语，他便接着说：「我想，警察总该把杰维斯·莱克斯顿殒命的那套公寓彻底搜查过一遍了吧？」

这时，阿尔弗雷德·芬奇突然插了话。

「没有，爱德华爵士，公寓没有搜查过。」他恭恭敬敬地答道。

===Original===

“Are you sure of that?”

“Quite sure. It is not usual to institute a search unless there is cause for suspicion against a person actually living in the house or flat where the murder has been committed. Now, the first C.I.D. man, who was in charge of the preliminary inquiries, undoubtedly formed the definite opinion that Roger Gretorex had poisoned Jervis Lexton. From his point of view there was no need to go further, the more so as his view was confirmed by a conversation he had with Gretorex just after he had taken a statement from Mrs. Lexton. The inspector, a day or two later on, interviewed Mrs. Huntley. You have that first statement of hers, gentlemen, in that bundle of papers I have laid down over there, marked ‘I.’ Mrs. Huntley then perjured herself, apparently because she had made a solemn promise to Dr. Gretorex to reveal nothing as to his association with Mrs. Lexton. She was, of course, quite unaware, at the time, of the fearful injury she was doing her employer.”

The Home Secretary opened the bundle marked “I.” He read through first the notes which Inspector Orpington had made during his first interview with Ivy Lexton﻿—that interview during which she had gone out of her way to volunteer the fact that Roger Gretorex entertained for her a hopeless, unrequited passion.

===Chinese===

「这一点您能肯定？」

「完全肯定。除非有理由怀疑实际住在凶案发生的宅子或公寓里的人，照例不会着手搜查。而负责初步调查的头一位刑事调查部人员，无疑已形成明确的看法：是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯毒死了杰维斯·莱克斯顿。在他看来，无须再查下去；更何况，向莱克斯顿太太录取口供之后，他与格雷托雷克斯本人的一番交谈，更使他认准了自己的看法。一两天后，探长又约谈了亨特利太太。她当时的第一份证词，就在我放在那边标着『I』字的那卷文件里，两位先生。亨特利太太那时发了假誓，显然是因为她曾对格雷托雷克斯医生郑重许诺，决不透露他与莱克斯顿太太的交往。她当时自然全然不知，自己是在给雇主造成何等可怕的伤害。」

内政大臣打开了标着「I」字的那卷文件。他先通读了奥平顿探长第一次约谈艾薇·莱克斯顿时所做的笔录——正是在那次约谈中，她特意绕着弯子主动说出，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯对她怀有一段无望的、单恋的痴情。

===Original===

Sir Edward next read most carefully again Mrs. Lexton’s two letters to Gretorex, as well as the letter which had reached him anonymously only yesterday.

“This Mrs. Lexton appears to be, in any case, a most hypocritical and abandoned woman,” he observed tartly.

Sir Joseph Molloy laughed a merry, hearty, boyish laugh, and Mr. Justice Mayhew looked round at him with an expression of shocked disgust on his stern face.

But “divil a bit,” as he said to himself, did Sir Joseph care for that.

===Chinese===

接着，爱德华爵士又极仔细地重读了莱克斯顿太太写给格雷托雷克斯的那两封信，以及昨天才匿名寄到他手里的那一封。

「这位莱克斯顿太太，无论如何，看来都是个虚伪透顶、自甘堕落的女人。」他语带尖刻地说。

约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士发出一串快活、爽朗、孩子气的笑，梅休法官则回过头去看他，严峻的脸上带着震惊而厌恶的神情。

可约瑟夫爵士呢——照他自己心里咕哝的那句土话，「一点儿也不」——满不在乎。

===Original===

“Do you agree,” said Sir Edward Law, looking at the Judge, “that these various documents provide sufficient reason for further inquiries?”

Mr. Justice Mayhew waited for what seemed a very long time, both to Sir Joseph and to Alfred Finch. Then reluctantly he answered:

“Yes, I think we have certainly cause here for the execution to be postponed, and for further inquiries to be made.”

“Now that we are on what I may term the right track,” exclaimed Sir Joseph, “I trust that my unhappy friend Roger Gretorex will not be allowed to languish in the cell of a condemned felon a moment longer than is absolutely necessary?”

The great advocate felt that he had now done all he could, and he was well aware that he had only been admitted to this conference by favour. And so, after a word of thanks to his old friend, and a sly look of triumph at the Judge, he went away, taking Alfred Finch with him, and leaving the Home Secretary and Mr. Justice Mayhew alone together.

===Chinese===

「您是否同意，」爱德华·劳爵士望着法官说，「这些各式文件，已为展开进一步查证提供了充分的理由？」

梅休法官沉吟了一阵——在约瑟夫爵士和阿尔弗雷德·芬奇两人听来，那真是漫长得可以。然后他才不甚情愿地答道：

「是的。我想，我们确实有理由在此暂缓行刑，并展开进一步查证。」

「既然我们如今已经走上了——恕我这么说——正轨，」约瑟夫爵士高声道，「我相信，我那位不幸的朋友罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，总不至于还要被摞在死囚牢房里，在绝对必要之外再多熬一刻吧？」

这位大名鼎鼎的出庭律师感到自己已尽了全力，而且他心里清楚，自己能列席这次会商，不过是蒙了破例的情面。于是，他向老友道了一声谢，朝法官狡黠而得意地瞥了一眼，便带着阿尔弗雷德·芬奇告辞而去，把内政大臣和梅休法官单独留在了一起。

---

===Original===

Inspector Orpington looked not only serious but also very grim, as, early that afternoon, and accompanied by the same colleague as had been with him here before, he rang the bell of Mrs. Lexton’s flat.

He felt extremely incensed for, turn the facts round in his mind as he might, there was no doubt that the childishly simple-looking, lovely little woman had completely taken him in. She had certainly, as he put it to himself, bamboozled him to the top of her bent.

Yet, even now, he found it almost impossible to believe that Ivy Lexton had poisoned her husband. Even so, he had been very much startled and impressed, not so much by Mrs. Huntley’s new statement, for he knew her to be a liar. No, what had astounded him had been Ivy’s letters to Roger Gretorex. Though these two letters had been written at a time when the writer was passionately in love with her correspondent, they revealed quite a different type of woman from what everyone connected with the case had taken her to be.

Inspector Orpington had also been unwillingly impressed by the letter written by Gretorex to Ivy in evident answer to one in which she had begged him to leave off coming to see her. The date, that of November the 6th, inscribed on Gretorex’s letter, proved that she had written the note to which it had been the answer after she had started her cruel work of poisoning her husband, if indeed she had poisoned her husband. The inspector realised that the letter was what might have been called a bull point in the writer’s favour. It breathed sincerity in every line.

===Chinese===

当天下午刚过不多时，奥平顿探长按响了莱克斯顿太太公寓的门铃，同来的还是上回陪他来过的那位同事。他不光神情严肃，而且面色铁青。

他只觉一腔火气无处发泄，因为任凭他把这些事实在心里翻来覆去地掂量，无可否认：那个模样孩子气般单纯、娇美可爱的小女人，把他骗了个彻彻底底。用他自己的心里话说，她把他糊弄得十足到家了。

然而直到此刻，他仍觉得几乎无法相信艾薇·莱克斯顿会毒死自己的丈夫。即便如此，他已大受震动、深感意外——倒不怎么是因为亨特利太太的新证词，因为他早知道那女人惯会撒谎。不，真正令他骇然的，是艾薇写给罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的那些信。这两封信虽写于写信人正热恋着收信人的时节，却揭示出另一种类型的女人，与全案有牵连的每个人原先认定的她，全然不同。

格雷托雷克斯写给艾薇的那封信，显然是答复她恳求他别再登门的一封便函；奥平顿探长也不由得为之动容。格雷托雷克斯信上落款的日期——十一月六日——证明她写那封被答复的便函时，已经开始了毒害丈夫的残忍勾当——倘若她当真毒害过丈夫的话。探长意识到，这封信对写信人来说，堪称一大要害论据。信的字里行间，透着一股真诚。

===Original===

It seemed a long time, to the two men standing there, before the door of the flat was opened by the cook. She looked surprised when she saw the inspector standing there, and then she smiled amiably.

“Want to see Mrs. Lexton?” she inquired. And, as he nodded, “Then want will be your master! She’s away in the country, and not coming back yet awhile.”

Orpington had already walked through into the hall.

“All alone in the flat?” he asked casually.

“I am this minute. There don’t seem any reason for keeping a young girl here all day just to do nothing,” said the woman tolerantly. “She works pretty hard when Mrs. Lexton is at home, that I will say.”

===Chinese===

对站在门外的两个人来说，仿佛等了老半天，厨娘才来开了公寓的门。她看见探长站在那儿，面露诧异，随即又和和气气地笑了。

「想见莱克斯顿太太？」她问道。见他点了头，又道：「那您可想也白想喽！她下乡去了，一时半会儿还回不来。」

奥平顿已经径直走进了门厅。

「就您一个人在公寓里？」他随口问道。

「这会儿可不就我一个人。让个年轻姑娘成天待在这儿闲着没事干，似乎没什么道理。」那女人宽厚地说，「莱克斯顿太太在家的时候，她干活是挺卖力的，这话我得说句公道话。」

===Original===

“Where is Mrs. Lexton staying?”

“I’ve got it down on a bit of paper. It’s near Brighton. A place belonging to the Lady Flora something or other. I’ll go and get it.”

“Wait a sec. We’ve come on what isn’t a very pleasant job, cook. We’ve got to search this place of yours.”

“Search this place?” Cook looked taken aback. “Whatever for?”

As no answer was vouchsafed to that question, “I’ll just go and tidy my room then,” she exclaimed. “I’ve been taking things easy since Mrs. Lexton went away. Where will you begin? How about the dining-room just here?”

===Chinese===

「莱克斯顿太太眼下住在哪儿？」

「我把它记在一张纸条上了。就在布莱顿附近。一个叫弗洛拉什么小姐的人的地方。我去取来。」

「稍等一下。我们来办的可不是什么美差，厨娘。我们得搜一搜你们这地方。」

「搜这地方？」厨娘显出愕然的样子。「到底为个啥呀？」

这个问题没人答理，她便嚷道：「那我就去拾掇我的屋子了。打莱克斯顿太太走了以后，我一直悠着干呢。你们打哪儿下手？就从这儿的饭厅开始，怎么样？」

===Original===

“All right. We’ll begin with the dining-room, and work down towards the kitchen.”

He added in a perfunctory tone, “No need to tell you to hide nothing, eh, cook?”

“There’s nothing to hide!” she exclaimed with some heat. “Everything’s always left open. Mrs. Lexton isn’t a lady to lock up her jewellery, like some do. She trusts us, and we are worthy of the trust, same as everyone is who is trusted.”

“If that’s so ’twill make our job easy. Then there’s no lockup at all?” and he looked at her rather hard.

“I keeps my box locked up, but you’re welcome to the key!”

“Don’t you be afraid. We’ll let your box alone. I meant, is there no lockup this end of the flat?”

===Chinese===

「好。我们就从饭厅开始，一路搜到厨房。」

他又用敷衍的口气补了一句：「用不着嘱咐您什么也别藏吧，呃，厨娘？」

「没啥好藏的！」她带着几分火气嚷道，「样样东西从来都是敞着不锁的。莱克斯顿太太可不是那种把珠宝锁起来的太太，不像有些人那样。她信得过我们，我们也配得上这份信任——谁受了人家信任，谁就配得上，一个理儿。」

「要是这样，我们的事就好办了。这么说，压根儿就没有上锁的地方？」他目光相当严厉地盯着她。

「我那口箱子是锁着的，可钥匙您只管拿去用！」

「您别怕。您那口箱子我们不动。我是说，公寓这一头就没有上锁的地方吗？」

===Original===

She waited a moment. “There’s half the big hanging cupboard in Mrs. Lexton’s bedroom always kept locked, just because there’s nothing in it. She keeps all her fine clothes﻿—my, and she has got a lot, fit to stock a shop with!﻿—in a little room that no one uses, next door to the bathroom.”

“Have you got the key of that part of the hanging cupboard?”

“I’ve never even seen it. But I expect it’s about somewhere. Maybe in the dressing-table drawer.”

It takes a long time to search a room thoroughly, and by the time the two men had done with the dining-room and the drawing-room, they felt tired.

===Chinese===

她顿了顿。「莱克斯顿太太卧室里那口大挂衣柜，有一半儿一直锁着——就因为里头啥也没有。她那些好衣裳都搁在一间没人用的小屋里，就在浴室隔壁——嗬，她那衣裳可多了去了，够摆满一间铺子的！」

「挂衣柜锁着的那半边，钥匙在您手里吗？」

「我连见都没见过。不过我估摸着总在这屋里哪个地方。没准儿在梳妆台抽屉里。」

把一个房间搜个底朝天，是要花上老长时间的；等两人搜完饭厅和客厅，已经觉出乏了。

===Original===

“Perhaps we’d better do Mrs. Lexton’s room next? Not that I expect to find anything there. The room in which that poor chap died was searched, and thoroughly too, though not till after the postmortem.”

Cook brought the bit of paper on which Ivy had written down her country address. Then she went off again into her kitchen.

The two men walked, in a rather gingerly way, into Ivy Lexton’s charming bedroom.

There the searchers had an easy task, for everything was unlocked, as the cook had said it would be.

But suddenly Orpington exclaimed, “Why, this must be the room where, according to that good old soul, there’s a lockup? I’d forgotten that! It’s the half of this big cupboard. Seen any keys about?”

===Chinese===

「接下来搜莱克斯顿太太的房间，是不是更好？倒不是我指望在那儿找到什么。那个可怜家伙死在里头的那间屋，已经搜过了，还搜得挺彻底——不过是验尸之后才搜的。」

厨娘取来了那张艾薇写下乡下地址的纸条，然后又回她的厨房去了。

两个人迈着颇为小心翼翼的步子，走进了艾薇·莱克斯顿那间迷人的卧室。

在那儿，搜查的人手到擒来，毫不费事，因为样样东西都没上锁，正如厨娘先前所说。

可是奥平顿忽然叫了起来：「哟，照那位好心老太太的说法，有上锁地方的，准就是这间屋了？我把这个给忘了！就是这口大柜子的一半。瞧见哪儿有钥匙吗？」

===Original===

The other shook his head.

Orpington, stepping back, looked dubiously at the big handsome inlaid piece of furniture. It was a fine bit of early Victorian cabinet work, and had belonged to the mother of the Miss Rushworth whose room this was. Though it was not in modern taste, Inspector Orpington thought it a beautiful object.

“I wonder if we’ve any call to force this lock?” he muttered to himself. “I wouldn’t like to hurt that cupboard in any way. It’s a good piece﻿—”

“I bet you I can open it all right without doing it a bit of harm,” said the other man confidently.

He went up to the cupboard. Then he did something to the lock with a bit of wire he happened to have in his pocket, and﻿—the big door swung open.

===Chinese===

同伴摇了摇头。

奥平顿退后一步，将信将疑地打量着这件气派的大镶嵌木器。这是早期维多利亚时代细木家具中的上品，原先是这屋子的主人家——拉什沃思小姐们——母亲的东西。虽说不合现代趣味，奥平顿探长却觉得它是件漂亮的物件。

「不知我们有没有必要硬撬这把锁？」他喃喃自语，「我可不愿意这柜子受到半点损伤。这是件好东西——」

「我敢跟你打赌，我能把它打开，一点也伤不着它。」同伴信心十足地说。

他走到柜子跟前，用口袋里恰好带着的一截铁丝对锁鼓捣了几下——柜门便豁然洞开。

===Original===

Orpington came forward quickly. He peered into the mahogany-lined cavity. It was empty, save for a shabby-looking red-leather despatch-box, on which, so faded as to be practically indecipherable, were embossed three gilt letters.

“That’s a rummy looking thing! One wouldn’t expect Mrs. Lexton would have such an object as this about,” and he lifted the despatch-box out of the cupboard.

It was surprisingly light.

“I wonder if she kept Gretorex’s love-letters in there,” said the other with a laugh. “If so, we may find something useful, eh?”

Orpington shook the box. Though it was so light he could feel that there was something in it which rolled about.

===Chinese===

奥平顿赶紧上前，朝那桃花心木衬里的柜腔里张望。柜里空空如也，只有一只看着颇为寒酸的红皮公文箱，箱面上凸印着三个烫金字母，只是早已褪色，几乎辨认不出了。

「这玩意儿瞧着可真古怪！真想不到莱克斯顿太太身边会有这么一件东西。」说着他把公文箱从柜子里搬了出来。

箱子轻得出奇。

「没准儿她把格雷托雷克斯的情书都收在这箱子里呢。」同伴笑着说，「要是那样，咱们兴许能找到点有用的东西，呃？」

奥平顿摇了摇箱子。箱子虽然那么轻，他却觉出里头有什么东西在滚动。

===Original===

“You won’t find it as easy to open this box as you did that cupboard,” he observed, “but it has to be done.”

“The only way we could open this,” said the sergeant, decidedly, “would be with a kitchen knife, unless they’ve got a chisel.”

“You go and get what you can from the old woman.”

A minute or two later the man came back. “She’s in her room tidying up,” he said with a grin, “so I just took this without saying ‘by your leave.’ ” And he held up a short stout kitchen knife.

“You just lock that door,” said Orpington quickly.

===Chinese===

「开这口箱子，可不像开那口柜子那么容易，」他说，「不过非开不可。」

「咱们能开这箱子的唯一法子，」警佐斩钉截铁地说，「就是用菜刀，除非他们家里有凿子。」

「你到老太太那儿去，能拿什么拿什么。」

一两分钟后那人回来了。「她在屋里拾掇呢，」他咧嘴一笑，「我就没打声招呼，顺手把这个拿来了。」说着举起一柄短粗的菜刀。

「你先把门锁上。」奥平顿急急地说。

===Original===

And then, the two men, by exerting a great deal of strength, managed to prize open the hinges of the old despatch-box which had belonged to Ivy’s father. The lock stayed fast.

The inspector felt a pang of disappointment, for there only lay on the rubbed green velvet lining a lady’s fancy handbag.

And then Orpington suddenly remembered Mrs. Huntley’s sworn statement. In that statement was actually a description of the bag Ivy had had with her when the old woman had found her alone in the surgery with the jar of arsenic on the table before her. A bolster bag that “looked like mother-of-pearl.” This was the same one without doubt.

He took the odd little bag out of the despatch-box and pressed the jewelled knob﻿—to find nothing in it but a cable from South Africa. The signature, “Rushworth,” meant nothing to him, though of course he knew of the famous Rushworth Line.

Then he opened the little white leather-lined, inner pocket of the bag. It, too, was empty. A faint scent, that of a popular face powder, rose from it.

And then, suddenly, he noticed, with a queer quickening of his pulse, that a few grains of what looked like kitchen salt clung to the white leather sides.

===Chinese===

接着，两个人使出了大力气，把那口艾薇父亲留下的旧公文箱的铰链撬开了。锁头却依然纹丝不动。

探长心头掠过一阵失望，因为磨旧的绿色丝绒衬里上，只孤零零躺着一只女士的精巧手袋。

这时奥平顿猛然想起了亨特利太太的宣誓证词。那份证词里，恰恰描写过老太太发现艾薇独自待在诊室里、桌上摆着那罐砒霜时，她随身带着的那只手袋——一只「看着像珍珠母做的」长枕包。眼前这只，毫无疑问正是那一只。

他把那只古怪的小包从公文箱里取出来，按了按那颗嵌珠宝的圆钮——里面除了一封南非来的电报，什么也没有。「拉什沃思」的署名对他来说并无意义，尽管那家大名鼎鼎的拉什沃思轮船公司，他自然是听说过的。

接着他打开了包里那个白色皮革衬里的小内袋。内袋也是空的，只飘出一缕淡淡的香气，是一种大众牌子的扑面粉的气味。

就在这时，他忽然察觉——心头一阵莫名的悸动——几粒看着像食盐的东西，粘在白色皮革的袋壁上。

===Original===

Moistening his finger, he put it against the leather, and a few grains stuck on to his wet finger. Face powder? No, not face powder. He touched his finger with his tongue, and then the colour rushed up all over his face.

“I’d like Sir Bernard to have a squint at that!” he exclaimed, holding up the open bag.

“D’you mean you’ve found something?” the other cried excitedly.

“Hush!”

Carefully Orpington put the rather absurd-looking mother-of-pearl bolster into a big black bag which he had brought with him. Then he put the empty despatch-box back into the cupboard.

===Chinese===

他濡湿了手指，往那皮子上一按，几粒粉末沾上了湿指尖。扑面粉吗？不，不是扑面粉。他用舌尖碰了碰手指，血色顿时涌上了他整张脸。

「我倒想让伯纳德爵士来瞧瞧这个！」他举起敞开的小包，喊了一声。

「你是说，你发现什么东西了？」同伴激动地喊道。

「嘘！」

奥平顿小心翼翼地把那只模样颇为可笑的珍珠母长枕包，放进他随身带来的一只黑色大提包里，又把空了的公文箱放回柜中。

===Original===

“Let’s get out of this,” he murmured, “before the old woman sees us. Can you manage to shut the cupboard as cleverly as you opened it?”

“I think I can,” said the other. And sure enough he did shut it, though it took him longer to lock up the half of the great early-Victorian cupboard than it had done to unlock it.

Orpington took a card out of his notebook. He wrote on it: “We’ve seen everything we wanted. Shan’t be troubling you any more,” and left it in a prominent place on the hall table.

Then he shut the front door rather loudly behind him, and, together, the two men went down the stairs by the side of the lift.

When they were safely out of the Duke of Kent Mansion, the inspector stayed his steps.

“I’ve got her!” he said exultingly. “Little Ivy will live to be sorry she bamboozled ‘yours truly,’ my boy!”

===Chinese===

「趁老太太还没瞧见咱们，赶紧离开这儿。」他低声道，「你能像开的时候那样利索，把它再关上吗？」

「我想行。」同伴说。果然，他真把柜子关上了，只是把那口早期维多利亚时代大柜的半边重新锁好，比开的时候费了更多的工夫。

奥平顿从记事本里抽出一张卡片，在上面写道：「该看的我们都看过了。不再打扰您了。」把它端端正正留在门厅桌上显眼的地方。

随后他颇响地带上了身后的前门，两个人一道顺着电梯旁的楼梯走了下去。

等他们稳稳当当地出了肯特公爵大厦，探长收住了脚步。

「这回可逮住她了！」他得意扬扬地说，「小艾薇糊弄了『鄙人』，往后有她后悔的日子呢，我的小伙子！」

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===

With a sudden cry of fear Ivy Lexton sat up in the Jacobean four-post bed, where she had spent a broken night.

She was still plunged in sleep, but anyone standing, say, by the large half-moon window of the delightful old-world country bedroom would have thought her awake, for her violet-blue eyes were wide open and dilated, as if with terror.

How lovely she looked; how childlike was the pure, delicate contour of her face, and the droop of her little red mouth. Her dimpled shoulders rose from what she called a “nightie” of flesh-coloured crêpe de Chine. The sleeveless bodice was edged with a deep band of real lace, and, to the eyes of the old-fashioned maid who waited on her in this, her friend’s, Lady Flora Desmond’s, country cottage, it looked more like a ball-dress than a nightgown.

There were tens of thousands of human beings who, had they been privileged to see Ivy as she was now, this morning, would have felt their hearts contract with intense pity for the woman they regarded as having been the innocent victim of an extraordinary set of ironic circumstances. There were also tens of thousands of other human beings who, though they had had strong doubts as to the part she had played in the singular story, would have told themselves that their suspicions had been cruelly unjust, could they have looked into that flower-like face, and heard the words now escaping from her half-opened mouth.

Those words were uttered in an appealing, broken tone, “Don’t hurt him! Please don’t hurt him!” And then: “Oh, Roger, I am so sorry for you!”

===Chinese===

艾薇·莱克斯顿猛地发出一声惊恐的喊叫，从那张詹姆士一世时代的四柱床（Jacobean four-post bed）上坐起身来——这一夜她睡得断断续续。

她其实仍沉在睡梦里，可是倘若有人——姑且这么说吧——站在这间古色古香、令人惬意的乡间卧室那扇宽大的半月形窗前，准会以为她是醒着的，因为她那双泛蓝的紫罗兰色眼睛睁得大大的，瞳孔涨开，像是吓坏了。

她这副模样多么可爱；那纯净娇柔的面庞轮廓，那微微下垂的小巧红唇，又是多么孩子气。她那对生着笑靥的肩膀，从她自称「睡裙」（nightie）的肉色双绉（crêpe de Chine）寝衣里露出来。无袖的衣身镶着一道宽宽的真蕾丝滚边；在这伺候她的老派女仆眼里——女仆是在她这位朋友、弗洛拉·德斯蒙德小姐的乡间小别墅里当差的——这件寝衣与其说是件睡袍，看着倒更像个舞会礼服。

成千上万的人，倘若能有幸见到此刻的艾薇——就在今天早晨——准会为她心头一紧，生出深切的怜悯，因为他们认定，这个女人是一连串异乎寻常的、反讽意味十足的境遇的无辜牺牲品。还有成千上万别样的人，尽管对她在那段离奇故事里所扮演的角色起过重重的疑心，可只要能看一眼这张鲜花似的脸，听一听此刻从她半开的唇间逸出的那些话，也准会告诉自己：自己的怀疑未免太狠心、也太冤枉人了。

那些话是用一种哀恳的、断断续续的声调吐出来的：「别伤害他！求求你们，别伤害他！」接着又是一句：「噢，罗杰，我真为你难过！」

===Original===

Ivy’s soul was not here in this delightful country bedroom. It had travelled a long long way, to a prison situated on the outskirts of London.

She seemed to be gazing through the door of a small, bare room, which she knew to be now occupied by one on whom judgment of death was to be executed that morning.

There stood by the pallet bed the tall, sinewy figure of a man who had loved her with a passionate and absorbing love, and whom she, in her own fashion, had also loved. By his wish, at his trial, not a word concerning what she had called their “friendship,” had been uttered in extenuation of anything he, Roger Gretorex, had done, or left undone.

There he stood, the man whose arms had so often cradled her, on whom she had made the limitless demands that a woman only makes on the man she loves. Never once, in great or in little things, had he failed her.

This morning he looked strange indeed, for though dressed, he was collarless, and clad in an old tweed suit﻿—a suit which Ivy remembered well, and which she had once told him caressingly she liked to see him wear.

===Chinese===

这会儿，艾薇的灵魂并不在这间惬意的乡间卧室里。它已经走了很远很远的一段路，去到伦敦郊外的一座监狱。

她仿佛正隔着房门，凝望一间狭小而空荡的屋子；她知道，屋子如今住着的，是一个当天早晨就要被执行死刑判决的人。

那张窄小简陋的床边，立着一个瘦高精壮的身影——这个男人曾以一种炽烈而倾尽心魂的爱情爱着她，而她，也以她自己的方式爱过他。依照他的意愿，在他的庭审上，关于她口中的那段「交情」，不曾有一个字被提起，来为罗杰·格雷托雷克斯的所作所为、或所不为，作一丝一毫的开脱。

他就站在那里——这个双臂曾无数次把她搂在怀里的男人，这个曾被她作过无限索取的男人；那样的索取，一个女人只肯对她所爱的男人去作。无论大事小事，他从不曾有一次辜负过她。

今天早晨，他这模样看上去实在异样：人虽穿戴齐整，却没有戴领子，身上是一套旧花呢衣裳——那套衣裳艾薇记得清清楚楚，她曾娇痴地告诉过他，她爱看他穿这一套。

===Original===

He held himself upright, with his head thrown back in what had been a characteristic attitude.

In Ivy’s vision two men were pinioning Roger’s arms, and it was to them that, living through this terrible nightmare, she had just addressed her piteous plea. And then with slow steps the chaplain, together with the governor of the prison, walked in.﻿ ﻿…

It was all happening exactly as Ivy had once seen something happen, in what had then appeared to her just a thrilling scene in a play, in London about a year ago.

And now Roger left the cell and began walking, with steady steps, his head still thrown back, down a narrow way.﻿ ﻿…

And then the woman in the bed gave a stifled shriek, for suddenly she saw the gallows through an open door at the end of the passage.

===Chinese===

他站得笔直，头向后仰着——那本是他素来惯有的姿态。

在艾薇的幻象里，两个男人正反剪着罗杰的双臂；方才她经历着这场骇人的噩梦，正是朝他们俩发出那声凄切的哀求。随后，监狱牧师（chaplain）同典狱长迈着缓慢的步子走了进来。……

这一切的发生，同艾薇从前眼见过的某幕情景一模一样——约莫一年前在伦敦，当时的她，只当那是戏里一个惊心动魄的场面。

此刻罗杰离开囚室，迈着沉稳的步子，依旧仰着头，顺着一条窄道走下去。……

跟着，床上的女人发出一声压抑的尖叫，因为她猛然看见，走廊尽头一扇敞开的门外，立着绞刑架。

===Original===

She covered her face with her hands, yet something seemed to force her to peep through her fingers and﻿—for a fleeting moment﻿—Roger Gretorex turned and looked at her.﻿ ﻿…

So had the condemned man turned and looked at the woman in the play.

But Roger’s face was so charged with mute, terrible reproach that, with an anguished cry of protest, Ivy awoke﻿—awoke to the blessed reality that she was sixty miles from the place where that awful drama was to be enacted this morning.

Her shaking hand felt for her diamond-circled watch on the Chippendale table standing by her bed. Having found it, she held it up close before her eyes.

It was only eight o’clock. She sighed heavily, for that meant that there was another hour of misery and suspense to be lived through. Nay, maybe even as much as an hour and a half﻿—for the morbid-minded woman pal of hers who intended to stand near the prison gate till the death notice was put up, and had promised faithfully to telephone to her from a house near by, had thought it unlikely she could get a trunk call through before half-past nine.

===Chinese===

她双手掩住脸，可仿佛有什么东西逼着她从指缝里偷看——就那么一刹那——罗杰·格雷托雷克斯回过头来，望了她一眼。……

当年戏里的那个死囚，也正是这样回过头来，望了那女人一眼的。

可是罗杰的脸上有那么一股无言的、可怕的责备，艾薇发出一声痛苦欲绝的抗议喊叫，惊醒了过来——醒回到有福的现实之中：此地离那出骇人的戏当天早晨即将开演的地方，足有六十英里。

她伸出颤抖的手，在床边那张齐彭代尔小桌（Chippendale table）上摸索她那块镶着一圈钻石的怀表。摸到之后，她把它举起来，紧贴到眼前。

才八点钟。她重重地叹了一口气，因为这意味着还有一小时的煎熬和悬心要挨。不，说不定要挨上一个半小时——她那位心思病态的女友，打定主意要守在监狱大门口，直到处决告示贴出来才罢，还一口应承，说要从近旁一户人家打电话给她；她也掂量过，九点半之前恐怕接不通长途电话（trunk call）。

===Original===

The tears began rolling down Ivy Lexton’s cheeks; yet it was not for Roger Gretorex, and his awful fate, that she was weeping. It was for pity of herself, for all she had gone through, and for what remained for her to go through, till she knew for certain that Roger Gretorex had died, as he had lived, silent.

She was well aware, deep down in her heart, that not only his counsel, Sir Joseph Molloy, who believed him innocent, but also that Roger’s mother, would hope up to the last moment of his sentient life that he would clear himself by shifting the burden of guilt on the one who was guilty.

Ivy had written the condemned man a letter three days ago, and she had made so many rough copies of that short letter that she knew it, now, by heart.

She repeated that touching letter over to herself, rocking her slight body this way and that in the large bed.

===Chinese===

泪水开始顺着艾薇·莱克斯顿的双颊往下滚；然而她哭的不是罗杰·格雷托雷克斯，不是他那可怕的命运。她哭，是怜惜她自己——怜惜她已然熬过的一切，还有摆在前头等她去熬的一切，直到她确确实实地知道：罗杰·格雷托雷克斯死了，正像他活着的时候一样，一声不响。

她在心底深处知道得清清楚楚：不仅他那相信他清白无辜的辩护出庭律师约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士盼着，就连罗杰的母亲也在盼着——一直盼到他神志尚存的最后一刻——盼着他把罪责卸到真正有罪的那一个身上，还自己一个清白。

三天以前，艾薇给那死囚写过一封信。这封短信的草稿她打了一遍又一遍，到如今，早已一字一句都记在心里了。

她把那封动情的信在心里默诵了一遍又一遍，瘦削的身子在大床上翻来覆去，一会儿倒向这边，一会儿又倒向那边。

===Original===

> Dear Roger,

> I am ill, so I cannot come to you. Otherwise I would do so. You know that I believe you innocent, and I want now to tell you how *grateful* I am for all your kindness to me, and for the love, however *wrong* it may have been, that you lavished on me.

> Ivy.

===Chinese===

> 亲爱的罗杰：

> 我病了，所以不能到你那里去。不然，我是会去的。你知道，我相信你是清白的；此刻我想告诉你，你一向待我的种种好意，你倾注在我身上的那份爱——无论它多么*不该*——都叫我多么*感激*。

> 艾薇

===Original===

She hoped that letter had given poor Roger pleasure and, above all, that he had read between the lines and seen how really, truly sorry she was﻿—how dreadfully grieved that everything had fallen out as it had fallen out. Indeed, she had twice underlined the word “grateful.”

And then she suddenly felt that she could not go on remembering any more. It was too horrible﻿—too horrible! So she took a bottle off the little table, where her watch was lying, and measured out a small dose into a medicine glass. Lady Flora would wake her, she knew, when that secretly longed-for message came through.

Soon she was once more plunged into uneasy slumber. But alas! again there came that hideous, hideous nightmare.

Once more she seemed transported to the condemned cell. But this time, in addition to the warders, the governor of the prison, and the chaplain, there was the horrid, cruel, fat-faced man, Sir Joseph Molloy, who had cross-examined her. True, he had dealt with her gently, kindly, but only because he had been adjured to do so, and, as well she remembered, with now and again a tigerish glare in his blue Irish eyes.

She listened with a feeling of indignation and pitiful dread to his voice uttering the words: “I adjure you, Gretorex, to tell the truth, now, for the sake of your poor mother who has always believed you innocent!”

===Chinese===

她但愿那封信给了可怜的罗杰几分欢喜，更要紧的是，但愿他读出了字里行间的意思，看出她是真的、真的难过——看出事情竟落得这般下场，她是何等痛心。说实在的，「感激」这个词底下，她画了两道线。

接着，她忽然觉得自己再也想不下去了。太可怕了——太可怕了！于是她从小桌上拿起一只药瓶——她的怀表就搁在那张小桌上——往药杯里量出一小剂来。她知道，那道她暗自渴盼着的音信一到，弗洛拉小姐自会唤醒她。

不多时，她又沉入了不安的睡眠。可是，唉！那骇人的、骇人的噩梦又来了。

她仿佛又被摄回了死囚牢房。只是这一回，除了看守、典狱长和监狱牧师，还有那个面目可憎、心肠冷酷的胖子——盘问过她的约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士。不错，他对她算得温和客气，可那只是因为事先有人恳切叮嘱过他；而且，她记得清清楚楚，他那双爱尔兰人的蓝眼睛里，时时会闪出虎狼似的凶光。

她又愤又惧，听他开口说出这样的话：「格雷托雷克斯，我恳求你，趁现在，看在你那可怜的母亲份上说出真话吧——她可是一向相信你清白的！」

===Original===

There was a pause. Ivy clasped her hands together in supplication. But the collarless prisoner had turned his sunken eyes away from her pleading face. Was he going to obey Sir Joseph Molloy? Yes! For she heard Roger’s deep voice answer: “It is as you have always thought it was, Sir Joseph. I die innocent. Ivy Lexton poisoned her husband.”

Outside that quiet bedroom Lady Flora, already on her way down to breakfast, heard a fearful cry﻿—“No! No! *No!*﻿—that isn’t true!”

She opened the bedroom door and saw that Ivy was asleep. “Poor child,” she murmured. “No wonder she talks in her sleep. Thank God! that unhappy man is to be hanged this morning.”

And, being the manner of woman she was, she offered up a silent prayer for the murderer, that he might make his peace with God.

===Chinese===

有一阵停顿。艾薇合拢双手，苦苦哀求。可那没戴领子的囚徒，已经把他深陷的眼睛从她哀恳的脸上转开了。他当真要听从约瑟夫·莫洛伊爵士了吗？是的！因为她听见罗杰低沉的嗓音答道：「事情正像您一向所认定的那样，约瑟夫爵士。我清白赴死。毒死我丈夫的，是艾薇·莱克斯顿。」

在那间静悄悄的卧室外，正下楼去用早餐的弗洛拉小姐听见一声可怕的喊叫：「不！不！*不！*——那不是真的！」

她推开卧室的门，看见艾薇还睡着。「可怜的孩子，」她喃喃道，「难怪她要说梦话。感谢上帝！那个不幸的人今天早晨就要上绞架了。」

而后，以她那样一个性情的女人，她又为那个杀人凶手默默作了一回祷告，求他能在死前同上帝修好。

---

===Original===

There came a sharp knock on the bedroom door, and Ivy woke with a stifled cry. She jumped straight out of bed and stood, her hands clasped together, waiting.

There came another knock, and then, “Come in!” she cried shrilly, and Lady Flora’s old parlourmaid entered the room.

Ivy had never liked the woman, and the woman had never liked her. She did not understand, and she never quite knew how to treat, those of her own sex whom she regarded as inferior to herself; yet some of the kindest letters written to her in the last few weeks had been from domestic servants, warmly sympathising with the heroine of their favourite Sunday paper.

“Mrs. Doghill is on the telephone, ma’am. Her ladyship is holding the line till you come.”

Ivy snatched up her periwinkle-blue satin dressing-gown and wrapped it about her. Then she thrust her little white feet into slippers that matched the dressing-gown, and ran downstairs, telling herself, not for the first time, how stupid it was to have the telephone in so public a place as the hall.

===Chinese===

卧室门上响起一记急促的敲门声，艾薇应声醒来，发出一声压抑的喊叫。她一跃下床，双手合握，站在那儿等着。

又是一记敲门声，随后她尖声喊道：「进来！」进屋来的是弗洛拉小姐家的老客厅女仆。

艾薇素来不喜欢这个女人，这女人也素来不喜欢她。凡是同性之中被她视为比自己低一等的人，她既不能理解，也永远拿不准该怎么相待；然而最近这几个星期里，写给她的信当中，有几句最体贴的话，恰恰出自家仆女佣们的手笔——她们满腔热忱，同情着她们最爱读的那份星期日报（Sunday paper）上的女主人公。

「多格希尔太太（Mrs. Doghill）来电话了，太太。夫人一直占着线，等您来接呢。」

艾薇一把抓起她那件长春花蓝的缎面晨衣裹在身上，又把一双白嫩的小脚塞进同色的拖鞋里，跑下楼去；一面跑，一面暗自埋怨——这已经不是头一回了——把电话安在门厅这么个众目睽睽的地方，实在是蠢透了。

===Original===

Lady Flora was standing, the telephone receiver to her ear. But when she saw Ivy she silently handed her the receiver and, turning into the dining-room, shut the door.

“Is that you, Millicent? Yes﻿—yes! I can hear quite well﻿—”

She waited in an agony of mingled hope and fear till, with startling distinctness, came the measured words that were being uttered sixty miles away.

“There’s been a reprieve. The story goes that important new evidence was laid before the Home Secretary yesterday.”

Ivy remained silent. She felt stunned. New evidence?

===Chinese===

弗洛拉小姐正站在那儿，听筒贴在耳边。可是一见艾薇，她便默默把听筒递给了她，自己转身进了餐厅，随手带上了门。

「是你吗，米莉森特（Millicent）？是——是！我听得很清楚——」

她在希望与恐惧交织的煎熬中等待着，直到那些六十英里之外一字一顿说出的话，清晰得骇人地传了过来。

「暂缓行刑（reprieve）了。据传，昨天有重大的新证据呈到了内政大臣面前。」

艾薇默不作声。她只觉得整个人都懵了。新证据？

===Original===

At last she managed to get out, in a low, strangled voice, “I﻿—I don’t quite understand.”

But instantly she heard a cross voice interject, “You’ve had six minutes﻿—can’t allow you to have any more now.”

“Indeed I haven’t! I’ve only just come to the telephone,” she said pleadingly.

“I can’t help that. The call was put through six minutes ago﻿—” And ruthlessly she was cut off.

===Chinese===

末了，她总算用一种低哑发紧的嗓音挤出一句话：「我——我有点儿不明白。」

可她立刻听见一个气冲冲的声音插进来：「你们已经讲了六分钟——现在不能再多给了。」

「我真的没有！我刚刚才走到电话跟前的。」她哀告道。

「那我管不着。电话是六分钟前接通的——」说罢，便无情地把她掐断了。

===Original===

Ivy turned towards the room where she knew her hostess was waiting, full of sympathy. She opened the door, and then she cried, “He’s been﻿—he’s been﻿—” and before she could say the word “reprieved” she had fallen fainting at the other woman’s feet.

Lady Flora would not have believed an angel, had an angel come and told her, that her dear little friend Ivy Lexton had fainted, not from relief, but from sheer, agonising fear.

Ivy spent the rest of the morning in bed, a prey to frightful anxiety and terror.

New evidence? What could that mean? Had Roger really failed her at last?

===Chinese===

艾薇转身朝那间屋子走去——她知道，女主人正在里头满怀同情地等着她。她推开门，喊道：「他已经被——已经被——」不等她把「暂缓行刑」这个词说出口，她已经晕倒在那位女主人的脚边了。

哪怕真有一位天使降临，亲口告诉弗洛拉小姐，说她亲爱的小朋友艾薇·莱克斯顿这一晕，并非因为如释重负，而是出于纯粹的、撕心裂肺的恐惧——她也决不会相信。

那天上午余下的时光，艾薇都躺在床上，任凭骇人的焦灼与恐惧轮番撕咬。

新证据？这话是什么意思？难道罗杰到头来当真辜负了她？

===Original===

At twelve o’clock the parlourmaid came in with a telegram.

> Hope to be with you tomorrow evening.

> Miles Rushworth.

===Chinese===

十二点钟，客厅女仆拿着一封电报进来了。

> 盼明晚与你相聚。

> 迈尔斯·拉什沃思

===Original===

The telegram had been sent from Paris the day before, and delayed in transmission.

“There is no answer,” said Mrs. Lexton in her soft voice. And then she lay back, feeling much less unhappy.

Whatever the mysterious reprieve might portend, Rushworth would very soon, in fact tomorrow, be here to help and to protect her.

As she read the telegram over for the third time, Ivy told herself how noble, how generous, how devoted the sender had proved himself. Also, what a wonderful life lay before her as his cherished, sheltered wife! Rushworth was all-powerful. New evidence? There could be no “new evidence” for the simple reason that nothing, nothing, *nothing* had ever happened﻿—that could possibly be found out.

===Chinese===

这封电报是前一天从巴黎发出的，在传递途中耽搁了。

「不必回电。」莱克斯顿太太柔声说道。说完，她向后一躺，心里痛快了许多。

无论这桩神秘的暂缓行刑究竟兆示着什么，拉什沃思很快——事实上就是明天——就要到这里来了，来帮助她，来保护她。

电报读到第三遍，艾薇在心里赞叹：发报的人证明了自己是多么高贵、多么慷慨、多么一往情深！还有，做他珍爱的、受他庇护的妻子，摆在她面前的将是何等美妙的生活！拉什沃思是无所不能的。新证据？不可能有什么「新证据」，道理再简单不过：从来不曾发生过什么，什么也没有，*什么也没有*——任何可能被人查出来的事，从来不曾发生过。

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===

As he walked up the gangway of the cross-Channel boat at Calais, Miles Rushworth’s heart was full of two women. The one was his dead sister, the other Ivy Lexton, the woman to whom he was hastening, and whom he expected to see today. Every fibre of Rushworth’s being longed consciously, hungrily, thirstily, for Ivy.

It was a source of real grief to him that these two could never now meet and love each other. He had been painfully aware that his sister hoped he would marry her own dearest friend, Bella Dale, and he had not dared to speak to her of Ivy.

During his long, dreary journey home he had often asked himself if all she had gone through had changed her from the deliciously pretty, kindhearted, rather irresponsible little creature he remembered her as being, into a more serious woman. Not that he wanted Ivy different. To him she was already absolutely perfect. But her letters had grown shorter, as his had grown longer, and vaguely they had disappointed him.

===Chinese===

迈尔斯·拉什沃思走上加来海峡渡轮的舷梯时，心里装着两个女人。一个是他已经亡故的妹妹；另一个是艾薇·莱克斯顿——他正兼程赶去相见的那个女人，他盼望今天就能见到她。拉什沃思浑身上下每一根纤维，都在有意识地、如饥似渴地思念着艾薇。

这两个人如今永远不能相见、不能彼此相爱了，这令他着实悲痛。他曾痛苦地深知，妹妹盼着他娶她自己最要好的朋友贝拉·戴尔，因而他始终不敢向妹妹提起艾薇。

在漫长而沉闷的归途中，他常常自问：她经受的那一切，是否已经把她改变——从他记忆中那个娇俏动人、心地仁厚、多少有些没心没肺的小可人儿，变成一个更加凝重的妇人。这并非说他希望艾薇变个样子。在他眼里，她本已十全十美。只是她的信越写越短，而他的信越写越长，隐隐地，那些短信让他失望。

===Original===

Roger Gretorex? How often had Rushworth tried to visualise the young man who had committed so dastardly a crime in order to set free the woman he had loved hopelessly, and without return, from the degradation of being tied to such a waster as had been Jervis Lexton.

Though even the South African papers had been full of the wretched fellow’s photographs, proving that he had a singularly handsome face, Rushworth had no clear vision of him. Also, Ivy had never once mentioned him in any of her letters.

Suddenly that fact, Ivy’s absolute silence concerning Gretorex, struck him as being strange. He also realised, what he had not realised till now, that poor lovely Ivy could not but be, all her life long, even after she changed her name, a marked woman. She would be always pointed at, and that wherever she went in English-speaking lands, as the heroine of a great cause célèbre.

Yet stop! In the circumstances, would it not only be right, but reasonable, that she should marry him, Miles Rushworth, almost at once? He would beg her, entreat her, to consent to an immediate marriage. And then he would take her away in his yacht to the South, to some quiet place where they two could be hidden in a trance of love, while people forgot the sordid story of the murder of which she had been the innocent cause.

===Chinese===

罗杰·格雷托雷克斯呢？这个年轻人犯下那样卑劣怯懦的罪行，为的是把一个女人从拴在杰维斯·莱克斯顿这种废物身上的耻辱里解脱出来——他爱那女人爱得无望，也全无回报；这样的一个年轻人，拉什沃思不知多少次想在心里描出他的模样。

尽管连南非的报纸都登满了这个倒霉家伙的照片，足见他有一张格外出众的俊脸，拉什沃思心里却始终没有他的清晰形象。再者，艾薇在任何一封信里都从未提过他。

突然，这个事实——艾薇对格雷托雷克斯绝口不提——让他觉得蹊跷起来。他也意识到一件直到此刻才意识到的事：可怜的、美丽的艾薇，此生此世，哪怕改了姓氏，也只能是个带着印记、受人指点的女人。无论她走到英语世界的哪个角落，人们都会对她指指点点，把她认作一桩轰动一时的名案的女主角。

可是且慢！处此境地，她几乎立刻就嫁给他迈尔斯·拉什沃思，岂止是正当的，不也是合情合理的吗？他要恳求她、哀求她，答应即刻成婚。然后他就带她乘上游艇南下去，到某个清静地方，让两人躲进一场痴迷的爱恋里，由着世人渐渐忘掉那桩肮脏的谋杀案——她虽是无心，却是那案子的肇因。

===Original===

It was a fine winter day, though bitterly cold, so the homecoming traveller found himself a comfortable spot in a sheltered place, on the upper deck of the steamer, where was just room for three.

Two deck-chairs were already occupied, one by a big man with whose powerful, humorous face Rushworth felt he was vaguely familiar, the other by a delicate, fragile-looking, little grey-haired lady. The third chair was unoccupied, and so he sat down in it.

Perhaps because he was in a sentimental mood today, he felt queerly moved when he saw that, under their rug, the big man was holding the hand of the grey-haired little lady. They were talking together eagerly, happily; obviously, so Rushworth told himself, an old-fashioned husband and wife, never so happy as when they were together.

His heart swung back to Ivy Lexton, and to the bliss of their coming meeting.

===Chinese===

那是个晴朗的冬日，只是冷得砭骨。于是这位归国的旅人在轮船上层甲板一处背风的角落里，觅得了一个舒服的位子——那里刚好容得下三个人。

两把甲板躺椅已有了主：一把上坐着个大个子男人，那副强悍而诙谐的面孔，拉什沃思觉得隐约眼熟；另一把上坐着位纤弱娇小、头发花白的夫人。第三把椅子空着，他便坐了下去。

也许是今天他正怀着伤感的情致，看见那大个子男人在毯子底下握着灰发小夫人的手，他心里涌起一阵奇异的感动。两人谈得热切而快活；显然——拉什沃思暗自判断——这是一对老派夫妻，从来没有比厮守在一起更快活的时候。

他的心又荡回艾薇·莱克斯顿身上，荡到他们即将相见的极乐里去。

===Original===

Poor, precious darling! What a terrible ordeal she had been through! He would regret all his life, all their joint life, that he had been far from her during the weeks that had followed the strange death of Jervis Lexton.

And then﻿—for a moment he thought his ears had misled him﻿—he heard that very name of “Lexton” uttered aloud by the man sitting one from him.

“That Lexton affair? Come now. If you really read your loving husband’s letters﻿—I sometimes suspect that you don’t, you naughty little thing﻿—well, there’d be nothing left to tell you! It’s hunting I should be today, instead of coming to meet an ungrateful woman.”

===Chinese===

可怜的、宝贝的心肝！她经历了一场怎样可怕的磨难啊！杰维斯·莱克斯顿离奇死去后的那几个星期，他竟远在她身旁千里之外——这一生，他们共度的一生里，他都将为此抱憾。

接着——有一瞬他以为自己的耳朵骗了他——他听见「莱克斯顿」这个姓氏，被坐在隔他一个位子上的那个男人大声说了出来。

「那桩莱克斯顿案？得了吧。你要真是把恩爱丈夫的信都读了——我有时疑心你压根儿没读，你这个小淘气——那我就没什么可告诉你的了！今天我本该去打猎，倒好，跑来接一个不知好歹的女人。」

===Original===

“I want to know what’s happening now, Joe. Also, most of all, what led to the extraordinary reprieve on the very day this man was to have been hanged?”

A reprieve? Miles Rushworth felt a sudden rush of anger and surprise. He was, of course, aware that Roger Gretorex, the man whose name and personality he loathed, and for whom he felt he would ever feel an intense, retrospective horror, was to have been hanged this very morning. That fact had been stated in both the daily papers which are published in English in Paris.

If it was true that there had been a reprieve that morning, how had this stranger already become aware of the fact?

===Chinese===

「我想知道眼下事情怎么样了，乔。还有，顶要紧的是，这人原定今天要被绞死，到底是什么缘故，偏偏在这一天颁下了那道出人意料的缓刑令（reprieve）？」

缓刑令？迈尔斯·拉什沃思心头猛地涌起一阵愤怒与惊愕。他当然知道，罗杰·格雷托雷克斯——这个他连名带人都憎恶的人，这个他自忖将终生怀有一种追想起来便觉剧烈的恐怖的人——本该就在今天上午被处以绞刑。巴黎出版的那两份英文日报，都登了这条消息。

倘若今晨当真颁下了缓刑令，这个陌生人又怎会已经得知了呢？

===Original===

“You know I told you, Eileen, long ago, that the poor chap had refused to appeal?”

“Yes, I remember that,” she murmured.

“Well, there seemed nothing left to be done! I was in despair, and it was only the day before yesterday that by﻿—well, I suppose old-fashioned folk like you would call it an intervention of Providence, some astounding new evidence was produced. And what’s more, I’ve been proved right!”

===Chinese===

「你知道，艾琳，我早就告诉过你，那可怜的家伙拒绝上诉？」

「记得，这个我记得。」她低声道。

「唉，当时看是再无办法可想了！我都绝望了。谁知就在前天，靠——呃，我猜像你这样的老派人会管它叫天意的干预吧——一桩惊人的新证据给端了出来。而且还有一层：事实证明我是对的！」

===Original===

And there was a tone of triumph in the, now low, organ-like voice.

“D’you mean that what you half suspected was true all along, Joe?”

She had turned her head round, and was gazing up into her husband’s face.

===Chinese===

那此刻已压低下来的、风琴般的嗓音里，透着一股得胜的调子。

「乔，你的意思是，你原先半信半疑的那件事，从头到尾都是真的？」

她已回过头来，仰望着丈夫的脸。

===Original===

Rushworth saw the big man bend his head as jovially he exclaimed, “Bedad! I think we’ve got her cold!”

A tremor ran through the lady. “What a horrible expression,” she murmured.

“Still, so far there’s something lacking, me dear, and it’s causing me a bit of anxiety.”

===Chinese===

拉什沃思看见那大个子男人乐呵呵地低下头，扬声道：「天老爷！我看这回咱们是把她攥死在手心里了！」

那位夫人浑身一颤。「这话多难听。」她喃喃道。

「不过，亲爱的，眼下还缺一样东西，这让我有点儿焦心。」

===Original===

“What’s that, Joe?”

“Motive!” the man exclaimed, in a voice that had become suddenly grave. And then he went on: “I don’t mind telling you that everything fair and﻿—well, a bit near the wind, also, was done to try and find out if our lovely, clinging Ivy had another man in tow. She is a”﻿—the speaker sought for something in place of the Biblical word trembling on his lips, but he gave it up, and said instead:

“We heard that there was one chap who went about with her a good deal last autumn, and who was far more often at Duke of Kent Mansion than Gretorex ever was. But though we ran him to earth and gave him﻿—at least I hope so﻿—a pretty bad quarter of an hour, it was clear that he would never have married her, not if she had been a hundred times free! Also, though he’s a gay bachelor, and manages to give his lady friends a scrumptious time, he’s not a rich man, and our practical little Ivy wants money, money, money all the time.”

===Chinese===

「缺什么，乔？」

「动机！」那男人脱口而出，嗓音霎时变得郑重。接着他往下说：「我不妨告诉你，凡是光明正大的手段——呃，也包括几桩险些擦着法律边的事——都使过了，为的是查一查咱们那位美丽的、缠人的艾薇身后是否还另拖着一个月人。她是个——」说话的人想另找一个词，来替换那个在他唇边打颤的《圣经》字眼，可他终于作罢，改口说道：

「我们听说，去年秋天有个月子同她厮混得挺勤，跑肯特公爵大厦的趟数，比格雷托雷克斯任何时候都多得多。可我们虽然把他追了个水落石出，还让他——至少我希望如此——好生难堪了一场，事情却很清楚：他是决计不会娶她的，哪怕她自由上一百回也不会！再者，他虽是个快活的单身汉，也有本事让他的女朋友个个过得称心快活，他却不是个有钱人，而我们那位讲求实惠的小艾薇，要的是钱，钱，钱，时时刻刻都要。」

===Original===

“Then what’s going to be done now? You don’t want your man, if he’s really innocent, to languish in prison half his life,” observed the little lady shrewdly.

“I do not,” he answered, in his rich, Irish voice. “What’s more, I want to shift that noose. Once we get her in the dock I’ll see there’s no recommendation to mercy; trust me for that! The woman’s a double-dyed murderess. She poisoned her husband, and she as good as hanged her lover.”

“You haven’t got her in the dock yet, and maybe you never will,” said his wife calmly.

===Chinese===

「那么现在打算怎么办？你要是当真觉得他无辜，总不至于愿意让他在牢里耗掉半辈子吧。」那位小个子夫人机敏地说。

「我不愿意。」他用他那浑厚动人的爱尔兰嗓音答道。「而且，我还要把那根绞索挪个地方。只消她一进被告席，我担保连一句从轻发落的宽宥附议都不会有——这一点你信我！这女人是个彻头彻尾的女杀人犯。她毒死了自己的丈夫，还等于是亲手绞死了她的情人。」

「你还没把她弄进被告席呢，兴许永远也弄不进去。」他的妻子平静地说。

===Original===

“Hold on! Hold on! Did you ever see me miss a kill I’d set my heart on? There’s another woman whose neck I’d like to wring﻿—that of an old charwoman, who, if she’d told the truth when Gretorex was first arrested, might have made all the difference, for there would still have been time, then, to find out something.”

“Has that poor, pretty woman had a chance of saying anything for herself?” asked his wife slowly.

“That artful little Jezebel is staying with a woman friend in the country at present, and the police are determined that everything is to be OK this time. It’s for hell she’s making﻿—” and he laughed a jolly laugh. “Ivy’s held all the cards in her hand up to now, but she’s going to lose the rubber.”

===Chinese===

「等着瞧！等着瞧！你几时见我放走过一心盯上的猎物？还有个女人的脖子我也想拧断——就是那个打杂老女佣，她要是当初格雷托雷克斯刚被捕时肯说真话，局面或许全然不同，因为那时还来得及再查出些东西。」

「那个可怜的漂亮女人，可曾有机会替自己分辩过一句么？」他的妻子缓缓问道。

「那个诡计多端的小耶洗别，眼下正住在乡下一位女友家里，警察这回打定了主意，事事都要办得妥妥帖帖。她这是一路奔地狱去呀——」说着他快活地哈哈一笑。「到如今，牌一直全攥在艾薇手里，可这一盘她要输掉了。」

===Original===

“I do wonder, Joe, what her motive can have been﻿—her husband had just got a good job, hadn’t he?”

For a few moments the speaker remained silent, then he said in a singular voice:

“If Gretorex had hanged this morning, I’d have betted a hundred to one that within a year we should have seen, in all the papers, a paragraph announcing that the beautiful Mrs. Lexton, whose husband had died in such tragic circumstances, was about to be married very quietly to Mr. Dash, a gentleman of great wealth and considerable position!”

===Chinese===

「我真纳闷，乔，她的动机究竟能是什么——她丈夫不是刚寻着一份好差事吗？」

说话的人沉默了片刻，随后用一种异样的声调说：

「要是格雷托雷克斯今早上了绞架，我敢拿一百对一打赌：不出一年，各家报纸就会登出一小段启事，说丈夫死得那样凄惨的美丽的莱克斯顿太太，就要悄悄嫁给某位某某先生（Mr. Dash）——一位豪富而地位显赫的绅士！」

===Original===

Rushworth moved slightly in his seat. He felt as if, within the last few minutes, the whole world, his world, had stopped going round, and that when it began again it would be in quite a different world that he would find himself.

“Then you think it will come out, now, that Mrs. Lexton was in love with some man﻿—?”

“I don’t think anything of the kind! My view is that among the innumerable young fools who have made love to her in the past year or two, she marked down some rich man as a possible husband, *were she only free*. One thing we learned only the other day. This was that two or three years ago she did her best to persuade that rotter Jervis Lexton to consent to an arranged divorce. He refused, unluckily for himself, for, though he was a poor mutt, he adored his wife. She’s the sort of woman over whom men go fantee﻿—”

“It’s unlucky that you can’t put a name to the happy man, Joe! Eh, my dear?”

===Chinese===

拉什沃思在座位上微微动了动。他觉得，仿佛就在刚才几分钟里，整个世界——他的世界——已停止了转动；而等它再转起来时，他会发觉自己置身于一个全然不同的世界。

「那么你认为，如今会真相大白：莱克斯顿太太一直爱着某个男人——？」

「我才不这么想！依我看，不过是近一两年里向她求爱的无数年轻傻瓜当中，她相中了一个有钱的，把他记下备作丈夫的人选，*只消她一旦恢复自由*。有件事是我们前两天才知道的：两三年前，她曾千方百计劝那个无赖杰维斯·莱克斯顿同意协议离婚。他拒绝了——这对他自己来说真是不幸，因为尽管他是个可怜的笨蛋，却深爱他的妻子。她就是那种男人见了要为她疯魔的女人——」

「倒霉就倒霉在你没法给那位如意郎君安个名字，乔！呃，亲爱的？」

===Original===

“Unlucky? I should think it is unlucky! Still, someone’s been supplying pretty Ivy with plenty of money during the last few weeks.”

“It’s strange she wasn’t suspected.”

“Of course she was! But not by the right people. My word, Eileen, she is a clever little woman! You should have seen her in the witness-box! Why, butter wouldn’t melt in her mouth. But she overplayed her part. I think a good many people found it difficult to believe that she’s been what she made herself out to be﻿—a kind of plaster saint.”

===Chinese===

「倒霉？岂止是倒霉！不过，这几个星期里，一直有人在给漂亮的艾薇大把地供钱。」

「说来奇怪，她竟没被人怀疑过。」

「她当然被怀疑过！只是没被该怀疑的人怀疑。我的天，艾琳，她可真是个聪明的小女人！你真该看看她坐在证人席上的样子！嘿，那模样，简直嘴里含着黄油都化不了。只是她的戏演过了头。我想不少人已经很难相信，她当真是她把自己装扮成的那种人——一尊石膏圣人。」

===Original===

“Is there any evidence to show that she was not that?” asked Lady Molloy quietly.

“There you go! Hate to believe anything of a certain sort about a woman! You’re a regular suffragette. A cold-blooded poisoner, yes﻿—but naughty? Oh dear no, not that, if you please.”

Half to himself, he added, “She wrote Gretorex two letters the poor chap’s servant got out of his paper-basket and pieced together. The minute I’d read ’em I knew there’d be a reprieve!”

===Chinese===

「可有什么证据，能证明她并非那样的人吗？」莫洛伊夫人轻声问。

「瞧你这话说的！凡是对女人的某种事，你就是不肯信！你真是个不折不扣的妇女参政论者。冷血的下毒犯，可以；可是不规矩？哎哟不，那个可万万不能，谢谢你的好意。」

他半是自语地补了一句：「她给格雷托雷克斯写过两封信，那可怜家伙的仆人从字纸篓里翻出来、又拼凑到了一起。这两封信我一读完，就知道准会有缓刑令！」

===Original===

“Why?”

“Because Eddie Law is a highly moral man. He loathes the sins he’s not inclined to.” Sir Joseph added candidly, “Most of us do, me dear.”

As Rushworth walked down the gangway at Dover two or three of his fellow-passengers nudged one another and smiled. They thought he was “a sheet in the wind,” for he did not seem to know quite what he was doing, or where he was going.

===Chinese===

「为什么？」

「因为艾迪·劳（Eddie Law）是个道德至上的人。他最憎恶的，恰是自己不想去犯的那些罪。」约瑟夫爵士又坦率地加了一句：「我们大多数人都是这样，亲爱的。」

拉什沃思走下多佛（Dover）的舷梯时，两三位同船的旅客彼此用胳膊肘碰了碰，相视而笑。他们以为他「喝了点酒」，因为他看上去简直不知道自己在做什么，也不知道自己要往哪里去。

---

===Original===

The moment Mr. Oram received the wire informing him that the execution of Roger Gretorex had been postponed, he hurried back to London, full of surprise and curiosity.

What an amazing﻿—he almost felt it to be, even now, an unbelievable﻿—story, was that told him with deferential clearness and dryness by Alfred Finch.

“God bless my soul!” he repeated at intervals.

And then at last, with some magnanimity, he observed, “Then you were right, Finch, and I wrong, all along. Though I thought her a vain little fribble, murder is the last thing I’d have suspected that young woman capable of.”

===Chinese===

奥拉姆先生一接到那封告知罗杰·格雷托雷克斯行刑已经延期的电报，便赶回伦敦，满心又是惊异又是好奇。

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇毕恭毕敬、条理分明而又不动声色地对他讲出的，是怎样一个惊人的——甚至此刻他还觉得简直难以置信的——故事啊。

「我的老天爷！」他隔一会儿便念叨一遍。

到后来，他总算颇有雅量地承认：「这么说，是你对了，芬奇，是我从头错到尾。尽管我原以为她不过是个虚荣而轻浮浅薄的小女人，谋杀却是我最不可能疑心到那位年轻女子头上的事。」

===Original===

“I didn’t suspect her either, sir. I was looking for a man﻿—the successor to Dr. Gretorex in the lady’s affections. The police believe they’re on the track of what may be styled Mrs. Lexton’s motive. It’s a man, right enough! Orpington wouldn’t tell me his name. He simply said he was very rich, and seemingly infatuated with her.”

He gave the old solicitor a rather odd look. But that gentleman did not take up the challenge, though all Rushworth’s cables, both to Ivy and to the lawyer himself, had been traced. And Mr. Oram had just become aware of the fact.

“Well﻿—well﻿—well﻿—”

===Chinese===

「我原先也没疑心她，先生。我找的是一个男人——在那位太太的芳心里接格雷托雷克斯医生的班的人。警察相信，他们已经追上了不妨称之为莱克斯顿太太的『动机』的线索。是个男人，错不了！奥平顿不肯告诉我他的名字。他只说他非常有钱，而且看样子为她神魂颠倒。」

他朝老律师投去颇为古怪的一瞥。但那位先生没有接这个话头，尽管拉什沃思发的一切电报——发给艾薇的，发给这位律师本人的——都已被人查了个底朝天。而奥拉姆先生也刚刚知晓了这一事实。

「哦——哦——哦——」

===Original===

“I suggest, sir, that you appeal to Sir Edward Law to consider the release of Dr. Gretorex on licence. The letter which he wrote to Mrs. Lexton﻿—you have a copy of it here﻿—which someone, I suspect one of the maids at the flat, sent anonymously to the Home Secretary, though one can’t exactly call it evidence, makes it clear to any impartial mind that our man was absolutely innocent of the whole business. As soon as she got busy, Mrs. Lexton wanted him out of the way. That’s as plain as a pikestaff. After all, he is a doctor, and he might have spoilt her game. Also he must have known, if he stopped to think a bit, that she had had access to the poison.”

“More fool he to go to the flat on that last day,” said Mr. Oram crustily.

It was that fact which, as soon as he had learnt it, had seemed to fix the guilt definitely on Gretorex.

“He was dotty about her! When a chap’s in that peculiar condition, sir, it’s as if he can’t keep away,” murmured Mr. Finch.

And then, it might have seemed irrelevantly, he observed: “Mr. Rushworth will be back in London in a day or two. Earlier, if he travels overland from Marseilles.”

===Chinese===

「我建议，先生，您去恳请爱德华·劳爵士考虑将格雷托雷克斯医生假释。他写给莱克斯顿太太的那封信——您这儿有一份抄件——虽然严格说算不上证据，却足以让任何不偏不倚的头脑看清：我们这个人在这整桩事里完全无辜。莱克斯顿太太一着手行动，就想把他除掉。这是一清二楚的事。毕竟他是个医生，他本可能坏了她的好事。再者，他只要肯停下来想一想，就必定明白：她是碰到过那毒药的。」

「他也真傻，最后那一天还跑到那套公寓去。」奥拉姆先生没好气地说。

正是这个事实，他一得知，便仿佛把罪责铁定地钉在了格雷托雷克斯身上。

「他对她迷得发昏！一个人到了那种境地，先生，就好像怎么也躲不开似的。」芬奇先生喃喃道。

接着，他似乎是没头没脑地插了一句：「拉什沃思先生一两天内就回伦敦了。要是他从马赛（Marseilles）走陆路，还会更早。」

---

===Original===

But even Alfred Finch felt a thrill of surprise when that same afternoon he was told that Mr. Rushworth was closeted with Mr. Oram. Indeed, he made a quick mental calculation. Either this must mean that their important client had come to Mr. Oram’s office straight from Victoria Station, or that he had flown from Paris.

Mr. Finch would have given a good deal to have been present at the interview which was taking place within a few yards of where he was working on a tiresome right-of-way case.

Mr. Oram’s head clerk had never much liked Miles Rushworth, and he could not help smiling to himself, as he considered the very awkward position in which that gentleman would find himself, if he was called as a witness for the Crown, as he most certainly would be if Mrs. Lexton were ever put on trial for the murder of her husband.

===Chinese===

然而当天下午，听说拉什沃思先生正与奥拉姆先生闭门密谈，连阿尔弗雷德·芬奇也感到一阵惊奇的震动。的确，他心里飞快地盘算了一番。这要么意味着他们这位重要客户从维多利亚车站（Victoria Station）一下车便直奔奥拉姆先生的事务所，要么意味着他是从巴黎飞回来的。

那场会晤就在离他几码开外的地方进行着，他正埋头办一桩恼人的通行权案；若能到场旁听，芬奇先生情愿付出不小的代价。

奥拉姆先生的主任书记员从来就不大喜欢迈尔斯·拉什沃思；此刻想到倘若那位先生被传为控方证人——而一旦莱克斯顿太太真因谋害丈夫受审，这是千真万确免不了的——他将陷入何等尴尬的境地，他不由得暗自微笑。

===Original===

Alfred Finch knew that something of a most incriminating nature had been found in Ivy’s bedroom, when the flat in Duke of Kent Mansion had been searched yesterday. He thought it probable that this consisted of a series of letters between Mrs. Lexton and Miles Rushworth. Even a man of huge wealth does not give something for nothing to an attractive woman.

Lawyers are apt to overlook the exception which proves the rule in life.

Had Mr. Finch been able to look through a blank wall, he would have seen Mr. Oram sitting at his writing-table, and looking across it straight at Miles Rushworth. And could he have heard what was being said, he would have realised that his employer was speaking in a tone that was, for him, oddly hesitant and uneasy.

“I’m sorry to say, Rushworth, that I’ve no doubt at all but that there’s been a terrible miscarriage of justice. I take it that you knew comparatively little of Mrs. Jervis Lexton, even though her husband was in your employment?”

===Chinese===

阿尔弗雷德·芬奇知道，昨天搜查肯特公爵大厦那套公寓时，在艾薇的卧室里找到了某样极具定罪性质的东西。他猜想，那多半是莱克斯顿太太与迈尔斯·拉什沃思之间的一批往来书信。家财万贯的男人，也不会白白送东西给一个标致的女人。

律师们往往无视生活中那个证明通例的例外。

倘若芬奇先生能看穿一堵实墙，他便会看见奥拉姆先生坐在写字台前，目光越过台面直直望着迈尔斯·拉什沃思。倘若他还听得见谈话，便会察觉他的东家说话的口吻，就他而言显得异乎寻常地迟疑不安。

「说来遗憾，拉什沃思，如今我毫不怀疑：这案子已经铸成一桩可怕的冤狱。我想，你对杰维斯·莱克斯顿太太想必所知甚少吧——尽管她丈夫在你手下做事？」

===Original===

Miles Rushworth made a conscious effort to appear calm and unconcerned. But he failed in that endeavour, and was aware that he failed.

“I knew them both fairly well,” he answered at last.

Mr. Oram began playing with a paper knife. He was wondering how much the man who sat there, with overcast face, and anxious, frowning eyes, was concerned with this horrid business.

===Chinese===

迈尔斯·拉什沃思竭力想装出平静淡漠的样子。但他没有做到，而且自己也知道没有做到。

「他们两个我都算相当熟。」他终于答道。

奥拉姆先生摆弄起一把裁纸刀。他暗自揣度，坐在那儿的这个人——面色阴沉、双眼焦灼地蹙着——同这件可憎的事到底有多深的干系。

===Original===

“What’s going to be the next move, Oram? I take it that you’ve been informed?”

“Well, yes, I have been informed, though quite unofficially. The﻿—ahem! authorities are very naturally perturbed. An innocent man was very nearly hanged. It was, in fact, a matter of hours﻿—”

Should he tell this old friend and client the truth? All his life long John Oram had cultivated caution, and technically he was now bound to silence. But he made up his mind that he owed the truth to Rushworth. Even now the solicitor had no suspicion of how really close had been the relations between the woman he now believed to have been a cold-blooded murderess, and this man whom she had so completely deceived. He was aware of how carelessly generous Rushworth could be and often was.

Still, a glance at his client’s face, now filled with a painful expression of suspense and acute anxiety, showed that this matter was of great moment to him.

===Chinese===

「下一步会怎么样，奥拉姆？我想你已经得到消息了？」

「哦，是的，得到消息了，不过完全是非正式的。当局——咳咳！——自然是惶惶不安。一个无辜的人险些被绞死。说实在的，只差几个钟头的事——」

该不该把实情告诉这位老朋友、老主顾？约翰·奥拉姆一生行事谨慎，而如今按规矩他本有守口如瓶的义务。但他打定主意：他欠拉什沃思一个实话。直到此刻，这位律师仍丝毫没有疑心到，他如今认定是冷血女杀人犯的那个女人，与这个被她骗得如此彻底的男人之间，关系究竟有多近。他也深知拉什沃思出手大方是何等漫不经心，且习以为常。

然而，朝主顾的脸上一瞥——那张脸上满是悬悬不安、焦灼难耐的痛楚神情——他便明白，此事对拉什沃思干系重大。

===Original===

“Mrs. Lexton,” he said in a low voice, “is going to be arrested, I understand, this evening, or tomorrow morning. The Criminal Investigation Department of Scotland Yard are completing what they consider a very strong chain of evidence against her. I have here copies of three letters which have come into their possession. You had better glance over them, Rushworth.”

He got up and, leaning across his table, handed a number of typewritten sheets to his client.

How strange looked those burning words of love and longing, transcribed on a bad old typewriting machine! But the man now reading them could visualise Ivy’s pretty flowing handwriting, and, as he read on, he turned hot and cold.

Then he started on Gretorex’s letter; the letter acquiescing in Ivy’s decision that there should be a break between them.

===Chinese===

「莱克斯顿太太，」他压低声音说，「据我所知，今夜或明早就要被捕了。苏格兰场刑事调查部正在收拢他们自认为针对她的极为坚实的证据链。我这儿有三封落入他们之手的信件的抄件。你最好过目一下，拉什沃思。」

他站起身，隔着桌子探过身来，把几页打字稿递给他的主顾。

那些炽热的倾诉爱意与渴慕的字句，用一台老旧的破打字机誊录出来，显得何等怪异！可此刻读着它们的人，眼前能浮现出艾薇那娟秀流丽的笔迹；他越读下去，越是一阵阵地发热、发冷。

随后他读到了格雷托雷克斯的那封信——那封默许艾薇决意断绝两人关系的信。

===Original===

“That was written,” observed the solicitor, “after Lexton’s mysterious illness was well started. I think you will agree that it is the letter of a man who was certainly unaware of what was going on?”

He waited a moment, then he added: “They’ve unluckily traced all your cables to the lady, Rushworth, as well as yours to me. I fear that you are certain to be called as leading witness for the Crown, if Mrs. Lexton is sent for trial, as seems now inevitable.”

“That would be monstrous! What is my connection with the case?” exclaimed Rushworth. “Surely I had the right to give all the help in my power to the wife of one of my own people?”

“They will call you in order to prove that Mrs. Lexton had a strong motive for wishing to get her husband out of the way,” returned Oram in a doleful tone. “I hope you refrained from writing to her? If you did, I trust she had the sense to destroy your letters.”

===Chinese===

「那封信，」律师说道，「写于莱克斯顿的怪病已经发作了些时日之后。我想你会承认，写信的人分明对正在发生的事一无所知吧？」

他顿了片刻，又接着说：「不幸的是，你发给那位太太的所有电报都被查到了，拉什沃思，你发给我的也一样。恐怕一旦莱克斯顿太太被移送受审——如今看来在所难免——你注定要被传为控方头号证人。」

「那未免欺人太甚！我跟这案子有什么相干？」拉什沃思喊道，「难道我没有权利尽力帮助我自己一名下属的妻子吗？」

「他们传你，是为了证明莱克斯顿太太有除掉丈夫的强烈动机。」奥拉姆凄然答道。「但愿你忍住了没给她写信？若是写过，我指望她有那个头脑，把你的信都烧了。」

===Original===

“It is this man Gretorex, if, as you seem to think, he is entirely innocent, who should be called, not I,” said Rushworth in a hard voice.

“Roger Gretorex will certainly refuse to give evidence against her. They’ll try to make him. But they’ll fail. He worshipped Ivy Lexton, and I fear he still loves her.”

Then the old man sighed. “It’s an awful story, Rushworth,” he observed, “however you look at it.”

The other threw the typewritten sheets of paper back on the table. He rose, and rather blindly he felt for, and found, his hat and stick.

===Chinese===

「该被传的是格雷托雷克斯这个人，不是我——倘若真如你所想，他完全无辜的话。」拉什沃思冷硬地说。

「罗杰·格雷托雷克斯必定会拒绝作证指控她。他们会设法逼他开口。但不会得逞。他曾经把艾薇·莱克斯顿当神明一样崇拜，而且我怕他至今还爱着她。」

随后老人叹了口气。「这是个可怕的故事，拉什沃思，」他说，「无论从哪一头看都是。」

对方把那几页打字稿丢回桌上。他站起身，有些茫然地摸索到自己的帽子和手杖。

===Original===

“I must be going now,” he said shortly. “If I’m wanted, you know where to find me, Oram.”

He felt humiliated to the depths of his being. His passion for Ivy Lexton had turned to bitter hatred. Yet he knew that their fates were linked together, and that through what had been his mad infatuation for this woman, a name which was known and honoured all over the world, was not only going to become a laughingstock, but also to be smirched and befouled forever.

As he went down the fine staircase of the old house, he exclaimed wordlessly, “By God, that shall not be!”

He waited a moment in the hall, and in that moment he thought of a way out.

===Chinese===

「我这就得走了。」他简短地说。「要是用得着我，你知道上哪儿找我，奥拉姆。」

他感到自己沦肌浃髓地蒙受了羞辱。他对艾薇·莱克斯顿的痴情，已化为怨毒的仇恨。然而他知道，两人的命运已拴在一处；由于他对这女人那场疯狂的痴迷，一个举世皆知、备受敬重的名字，不仅将沦为笑柄，还将永远被玷污、被糟蹋。

走下那座老宅气派的楼梯时，他心中无声地呐喊：「上帝作证，绝不容许这样！」

他在门厅里站了片刻，就在这片刻间，他想出了一条脱身之路。

===Original===

It was a way made possible by the fact that an unpleasant experience at the beginning of August, 1914, had taught him the value of gold. Since the Saturday which had preceded the outbreak of war, he had always kept a thousand pounds in gold, and a thousand pounds in Bank of England ten-pound notes, in the private safe of his London office.

He walked quickly to the corner of a quiet street where he had left his car, and threw the chauffeur the address.

Then he looked at his watch. If what old Oram had said was true with regard to the probable arrest of Ivy Lexton, there was just time to accomplish that which he had planned to do in what had seemed but one flashing second.

“Stop at the nearest telephone box,” he called out. And the chauffeur drew up at a tube station.

===Chinese===

这条路之所以走得通，是因为一九一四年（1914）八月初的一段不愉快经历，教会了他黄金的价值。自开战前的那个星期六起，他伦敦办事处的私人保险柜里，就一直存着一千镑金币，还有一千镑英格兰银行的十镑钞票。

他快步走到一条安静街巷的拐角——他的汽车停在那里——把一个地址丢给了司机。

然后他看了看表。倘若老奥拉姆关于艾薇·莱克斯顿很可能被捕所说的话属实，那么要办成那件他仿佛只在一闪念间筹划好的事，时间刚刚来得及。

「在最近的电话亭停一下。」他喊道。司机便把车停在了一座地铁车站旁。

===Original===

Rushworth was in the telephone box for a long time, for he had to a certain extent to speak in parables. But the young man whom he had called up, and had had the good fortune to find at home, at last understood exactly what was wanted of him. He was an airman to whom Rushworth had once been magnificently generous.

“Right-ho!” came the young voice down the line. “I’ll be quite ready. I understand you want me to take my wife, too, and that you’ll motor her down here from town. Her passport’s always OK. You can trust me. Afraid? Not much!”

Rushworth’s face looked strained and white as he came out of the telephone box.

He was well aware that he was inciting that lad to do, from pure gratitude, a very wrong thing. Well? If it “didn’t come off,” he, Rushworth, would take all the blame, of course. But he felt pretty sure that the plan he had made would succeed, for it had the two essential qualities which spell success. His plan was bold and his plan was simple.

===Chinese===

拉什沃思在电话亭里待了很久，因为多少得靠打哑谜来说话。不过他打给的那个年轻人——运气好，正巧在家——终于完全明白了要他做什么。他是个飞行员，拉什沃思曾对他有过一次极为豪爽的慷慨相助。

「好嘞！」电话那头传来年轻的声音。「我一定一切就绪。我明白了，您要我把太太也捎上，由您用汽车把她从城里送下来。她的护照一向是现成的。您尽管放心。害怕？才不呢！」

从电话亭里出来时，拉什沃思的脸紧绷着，煞白。

他心里清楚，他这是在怂恿那小伙子纯粹出于感激，去做一件大错特错的事。那又如何？倘若事情「砸了」，他拉什沃思自然会把全部罪责揽下。但他相当有把握，自己定下的计划会成功，因为它具备拼出「成功」二字的两样要素：他的计划大胆，他的计划简单。

===Original===

True, he wondered uncomfortably if the police had traced that last wire of his from Paris. He was glad indeed that Ivy had had the wit to telegraph her country address. And then, as he evoked her lovely face, her beckoning eyes, his own darkened, and filled with wrath and pain.

He did not go himself into his London office. Instead he sent in his chauffeur, with the key of his private safe, and armed with minute instructions as to what he was to take out of it.

Then, when the man had brought him the heavy little canvas bag, and the envelope containing a hundred ten-pound notes, he threw him the address of some lodgings in a quiet street off Piccadilly, where he knew Lady Dale and her daughter were staying just now. His sister had made him promise that he would see Bella the moment he reached London, and he was fulfilling that promise.

When he was told that her ladyship was out, but that Miss Dale was in, and alone, he suddenly felt as if his luck was holding, after all!

===Chinese===

不错，他心里隐隐不安，不知警方是否已追查到他巴黎发出的那最后一封电报。他着实庆幸艾薇机灵，电报里给的是乡下的地址。随后，当他唤起她那张可爱的脸、那双含情召唤的眼睛时，他自己的脸沉了下来，盛满了愤怒与痛苦。

他没有亲自走进自己的伦敦办事处，而是打发司机进去，交给他私人保险柜的钥匙，又细细叮咛了要取出哪些东西。

等司机给他拿来那只沉甸甸的小帆布袋，和装着一百张十镑钞票的信封，他又把一个地址丢给了他——皮卡迪利附近一条安静街上的几间寓所，他知道戴尔夫人和她的女儿此刻正住在那里。他妹妹生前要他答应，一到伦敦就去看贝拉，如今他正是在践约。

听说夫人外出了，戴尔小姐倒在家，而且独自一个，他忽然觉得，自己的运气终究还站在他这边！

---

===Original===

Ivy had insisted on coming back to London before luncheon.

Not only was her mind now full of vague, unsubstantial fears, but she was aware that Miles Rushworth would call at her flat some time this evening. That, indeed, was a fact to which she clung and constantly returned with a feeling of reassurance and hope. Even so she had not allowed Lady Flora to telephone the fact that she was returning unexpectedly to the flat. She felt, somehow, that she wanted no one to know about her movements just now. She was beginning to feel that most terrifying of sensations﻿—that of being hunted.

Even when settled comfortably, and alone, in a first-class carriage of the train taking her to town, she found she could not rest, and she actually got up and began moving about.

It was such an awful sensation﻿—that of feeling that human hounds might be hot on her scent.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

艾薇坚持赶在午饭前回到伦敦。

如今她心里不仅塞满了模糊缥缈的恐惧，而且她知道，迈尔斯·拉什沃思今晚某个时候会到公寓来看她。这一点，确是她紧抓不放、时时回望而心生宽慰与指望的一件事。即便如此，她也不许弗洛拉小姐打电话告诉别人她要突然回公寓。不知怎的，她觉得眼下自己的行踪谁也别让知道。她开始尝到那种最骇人的滋味——被人追猎的滋味。

即使独自舒舒服服地安坐在载她进城的一等车厢里，她也安不下心，竟真的站起身来，在车厢里走动起来。

那滋味实在可怕——总觉得一群人形猎犬正紧嗅着她的踪迹穷追不舍。……
